## I / 第一章

===Original===
Until he was almost ten the name stuck to him. He had literally to fight his way free of it. From So Big (of fond and infantile derivation) it had been condensed into Sobig. And Sobig DeJong, in all its consonantal disharmony, he had remained until he was a ten-year-old schoolboy in that incredibly Dutch district southwest of Chicago known first as New Holland and later as High Prairie. At ten, by dint of fists, teeth, copper-toed boots, and temper, he earned the right to be called by his real name, Dirk DeJong. Now and then, of course, the nickname bobbed up and had to be subdued in a brief and bitter skirmish. His mother, with whom the name had originated, was the worst offender. When she lapsed he did not, naturally, use schoolyard tactics on her. But he sulked and glowered portentously and refused to answer, though her tone, when she called him So Big, would have melted the heart of any but that natural savage, a boy of ten.

===Chinese===
这名字一直粘着他，直到快十岁。他是实打实打了一场架才挣脱它的。「这—么大」（So Big，出自宠溺逗弄的婴儿呓语）先是缩成了「这么大」（Sobig）；于是他便顶着「这么大」·德容——辅音磕绊、全不和谐——这么个名字，一直叫到十岁，那时他已是小学生，住在芝加哥西南一片荷兰味浓得化不开的地界上；那地方起初叫新荷兰（New Holland），后来叫高草原（High Prairie）。十岁上，凭着拳头、牙齿、包铜头的靴子和一腔火气，他挣得了让人以真名相称的权利——德克·德容（Dirk DeJong）。当然，那诨名时不时还会冒上来，得用一场短促而惨烈的遭遇战才能把它镇压下去。他母亲——这名字原本就是她起的——是屡教不改的头号惯犯。她口滑的时候，他自然不便把学校操场上的手段使到她身上，只是阴沉沉地板起脸，煞有介事地怒目相向，拒不应声；虽然她唤他「这—么大」时的那副声气，换了谁的心都要化掉，唯独化不动十岁男孩这天生的野人。

===Original===
The nickname had sprung from the early and idiotic question invariably put to babies and answered by them, with infinite patience, through the years of their infancy.

===Chinese===
这诨名源自一句人们早早就要问的傻话——大人无一例外拿它去问婴孩，而婴孩也以无限的耐心，在整个襁褓岁月里一遍遍作答。

===Original===
Selina DeJong, darting expertly about her kitchen, from washtub to baking board, from stove to table, or, if at work in the fields of the truck farm, straightening the numbed back for a moment’s respite from the close-set rows of carrots, turnips, spinach, or beets over which she was labouring, would wipe the sweat beads from nose and forehead with a quick duck of her head in the crook of her bent arm. Those great fine dark eyes of hers would regard the child perched impermanently on a little heap of empty potato sacks, one of which comprised his costume. He was constantly detaching himself from the parent sack heap to dig and burrow in the rich warm black loam of the truck garden. Selina DeJong had little time for the expression of affection. The work was always hot at her heels. You saw a young woman in a blue calico dress, faded and earth-grimed. Between her eyes was a driven look as of one who walks always a little ahead of herself in her haste. Her dark abundant hair was skewered into a utilitarian knob from which soft loops and strands were constantly escaping, to be pushed back by that same harried ducking gesture of head and bent arm. Her hands, for such use, were usually too crusted and inground with the soil into which she was delving. You saw a child of perhaps two years, dirt-streaked, sunburned, and generally otherwise defaced by those bumps, bites, scratches, and contusions that are the common lot of the farm child of a mother harried by work. Yet, in that moment, as the woman looked at the child there in the warm moist spring of the Illinois prairie land, or in the cluttered kitchen of the farmhouse, there quivered and vibrated between them and all about them an aura, a glow, that imparted to them and their surroundings a mystery, a beauty, a radiance.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜·德容（Selina DeJong）在自家厨房里麻利地穿梭——从洗衣盆到揉面板，从炉灶到餐桌；若是在蔬菜农场的田里做活，便从密匝匝的胡萝卜、芜菁、菠菜或甜菜垄间直一直冻僵了的腰身，稍事喘息，随即脑袋往弯着的臂弯里一埋，飞快蹭去鼻尖和额上的汗珠。她那双又大又亮的黑眼睛，会望一望那孩子——他临时蹲踞在一小堆空土豆袋上，其中一只便是他的全部衣装。他三天两头从那堆母袋子堆里挣脱出来，钻进菜园温软肥沃的黑土里刨的刨、拱的拱。塞莉娜·德容很少有工夫表达爱抚。活计总在她脚后跟火热地撵着。你看见的是一个穿蓝印花布裙的年轻女人，裙子褪了色、沾着泥。她眉宇间有一股被驱赶的神色，仿佛一个人行色匆匆，总走得比自己超前小半步。她一头浓密的黑发挽成一个讲究实用的髻，柔软的发圈发缕不住散落，又被她用那副头一低、臂一弯的忙乱手势拂回去。至于那双手，干这种活计时多半结满泥壳、嵌满泥垢——她正翻掘的泥土早钻进掌纹里去了。你看见的是一个两岁上下的孩子，浑身泥道子，晒得黢黑，还添着肿块、虫咬、抓痕和青瘀——被活计追赶的母亲手下的农家娃，这些原是人人有份的常例。然而在那一刻，当女人在伊利诺伊草原温暖湿润的春光里、或在农舍凌乱的厨房里望着那孩子时，他们之间、他们四周，颤动着、荡漾开一圈光晕、一泓暖辉，赋予他们和他们的周遭一种神秘、一种美、一种光华。

===Original===
“How big is baby?” Selina would demand, senselessly. “How big is my man?”

===Chinese===
「宝宝有多大呀？」塞莉娜会傻里傻气地问。「我的大男人有多大呀？」

===Original===
The child would momentarily cease to poke plump fingers into the rich black loam. He would smile a gummy though slightly weary smile and stretch wide his arms. She, too, would open her tired arms wide, wide. Then they would say in a duet, his mouth a puckered pink petal, hers quivering with tenderness and a certain amusement, “*So‑o‑o‑o* big!” with the voice soaring on the prolonged vowel and dropping suddenly with the second word. Part of the game. The child became so habituated to this question that sometimes, if Selina happened to glance round at him suddenly in the midst of her task, he would take his cue without the familiar question being put and would squeal his “So-o-o-o big!” rather absently, in dutiful solo. Then he would throw back his head and laugh a triumphant laugh, his open mouth a coral orifice. She would run to him, and swoop down upon him, and bury her flushed face in the warm moist creases of his neck, and make as though to devour him. “So big!”

===Chinese===
孩子会暂时歇下手，不再把胖乎乎的手指往肥沃的黑土里戳。他会咧开牙床——略略带点疲沓地——笑一笑，把双臂张得开开的。她也会把自己疲惫的双臂张开，开开的。然后两人齐声喊道——他的小嘴噘成一片粉红的花瓣，她的嘴唇因怜爱兼几分好笑而颤动——「*这——么——*大！」嗓音在那串拖长的元音上扶摇而起，到第二个字又骤然跌落。这是游戏的一部分。孩子对这一问题熟到了家，有时塞莉娜干活干到一半忽然回头看他一眼，他不等那句熟悉的话出口便会接住话头，心不在焉又尽忠职守地独唱一声「这——么——大——！」唱罢仰头大笑，笑得洋洋得意，张开的小嘴像一洞珊瑚。她便向他奔去，向他俯冲下来，把通红的脸埋进他脖颈间温热湿润的褶皱里，作势要把他一口吞吃掉。「这么大！」

===Original===
But of course he wasn’t. He wasn’t as big as that. In fact, he never became as big as the wide-stretched arms of her love and imagination would have had him. You would have thought she should have been satisfied when, in later years, he was the Dirk DeJong whose name you saw (engraved) at the top of heavy cream linen paper, so rich and thick and stiff as to have the effect of being starched and ironed by some costly American business process; whose clothes were made by Peter Peel, the English tailor; whose roadster ran on a French chassis; whose cabinet held mellow Italian vermouth and Spanish sherry; whose wants were served by a Japanese houseman; whose life, in short, was that of the successful citizen of the Republic. But she wasn’t. Not only was she dissatisfied: she was at once remorseful and indignant, as though she, Selina DeJong, the vegetable peddler, had been partly to blame for this success of his, and partly cheated by it.

===Chinese===
可他当然没有那么大。他没有那么大。事实上，他从来也没有长到那么大——长到她那爱与想象张开到极限的双臂巴望他有的那么大。你也许以为，多年以后，当他成了那样的德克·德容——名字（雕版印的）端在厚实乳白的亚麻信纸顶上，那纸丰厚挺括，看着像经某种昂贵的美国商业工序上了浆、熨过似的；衣裳出自英国裁缝彼得·皮尔（Peter Peel）之手；双座跑车装着法国底盘；酒柜里存着醇和的意大利味美思和西班牙雪莉酒；起居杂务由一位日本男仆伺候；总之，过的是共和国成功公民的日子——她该心满意足了。可她没有。何止是不满：她同时又悔又恼，仿佛她，塞莉娜·德容，卖菜的女人，对儿子的这份成功一半难辞其咎，一半又被它坑走了什么。

===Original===
When Selina DeJong had been Selina Peake she had lived in Chicago with her father. They had lived in many other cities as well. In Denver during the rampant ’80s. In New York when Selina was twelve. In Milwaukee briefly. There was even a San Francisco interlude which was always a little sketchy in Selina’s mind and which had ended in a departure so hurried as to bewilder even Selina who had learned to accept sudden comings and abrupt goings without question. “Business,” her father always said. “Little deal.” She never knew until the day of his death how literally the word deal was applicable to his business transactions. Simeon Peake, travelling the country with his little daughter, was a gambler by profession, temperament, and natural talents. When in luck they lived royally, stopping at the best hotels, eating strange, succulent sea-viands, going to the play, driving in hired rigs (always with two horses. If Simeon Peake had not enough money for a two-horse equipage he walked). When fortune hid her face they lived in boarding houses, ate boardinghouse meals, wore the clothes bought when Fortune’s breath was balmy. During all this time Selina attended schools, good, bad, private, public, with surprising regularity considering her nomadic existence. Deep-bosomed matrons, seeing this dark-eyed serious child seated alone in a hotel lobby or boardinghouse parlour, would bend over her in solicitous questioning.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜·德容做姑娘时叫塞莉娜·皮克（Selina Peake），那时她跟着父亲住在芝加哥。他们也在许多别的城市住过。野性勃发的80年代住在丹佛。塞莉娜十二岁那年住在纽约。在密尔沃基小住过一阵。甚至还有一段旧金山的小插曲——它在塞莉娜的记忆里始终影影绰绰，收场又仓促得连塞莉娜都发懵，尽管她早已学会对说来就来、说走就走的行踪不问一句。「生意。」她父亲总是这么说。「小小一票。」直到他死的那天，她才知道「一票」这个词用在父亲的生意上是何等贴切。西缅·皮克（Simeon Peake）带着小女儿走南闯北，凭职业、凭性情、凭天分，是个赌徒。手气顺时，他们的日子过得像王侯：下榻上等旅馆，吃着新奇怪异、肥美多汁的海味，看戏，雇车兜风（永远要双马拉车。要是钱不够雇一乘双马的车驾，西缅·皮克宁可步行）。时运遮起脸来，他们便住供膳公寓，吃包饭，穿时运和煦时置下的衣裳。这期间塞莉娜一直上学——好的、坏的、私立的、公立的——以她那种迁徙生活而论，规律得叫人吃惊。胸脯丰厚的太太们，看见这个黑眼睛、一脸严肃的孩子独自坐在旅馆大厅或公寓客厅里，总会俯下身去，关切地打听。

===Original===
“Where is your mamma, little girl?”

===Chinese===
「你的妈妈呢，小姑娘？」

===Original===
“She is dead,” Selina would reply, politely and composedly.

===Chinese===
「她死了。」塞莉娜会彬彬有礼、安安静静地回答。

===Original===
“Oh, my poor little dear!” Then, with a warm rush, “Don’t you want to come and play with my little girl? She loves little girls to play with. H’m?” The *m* of the interrogation held hummingly, tenderly.

===Chinese===
「哦，我可怜的小宝贝！」接着又一股热乎劲儿涌上来：「你不想到我家来，跟我的小女儿一块儿玩吗？她顶喜欢跟小姑娘玩了。嗯？」那问句收尾的 *m* 音，嗡嗡的，柔柔的，拖得老长。

===Original===
“No, thank you very much. I’m waiting for my father. He would be disappointed not to find me here.”

===Chinese===
「不啦，多谢。我在等爸爸。他回来要是找不着我，会失望的。」

===Original===
These good ladies wasted their sympathy. Selina had a beautiful time. Except for three years, to recall which was to her like entering a sombre icy room on leaving a warm and glowing one, her life was free, interesting, varied. She made decisions usually devolving upon the adult mind. She selected clothes. She ruled her father. She read absorbedly books found in boardinghouse parlours, in hotels, in such public libraries as the times afforded. She was alone for hours a day, daily. Frequently her father, fearful of loneliness for her, brought her an armful of books and she had an orgy, dipping and swooping about among them in a sort of gourmand’s ecstasy of indecision. In this way, at fifteen, she knew the writings of Byron, Jane Austen, Dickens, Charlotte Brontë, Felicia Hemans. Not to speak of Mrs. E. D. E. N. Southworth, Bertha M. Clay, and that good fairy of the scullery, the *Fireside Companion*, in whose pages factory girls and dukes were brought together as inevitably as steak and onions. These last were, of course, the result of Selina’s mode of living, and were loaned her by kindhearted landladies, chambermaids, and waitresses all the way from California to New York.

===Chinese===
这些好心的太太们白费了同情。塞莉娜的日子过得美极了。除了那三年——回想起来，就像从一间温暖明亮的屋子一头扎进一间阴冷彻骨的房间——她的生活自由、有趣、多姿多彩。她做的都是通常落到大人头上才做的决断。衣裳她自己挑。父亲归她管。她如饥似渴地读在公寓客厅里、旅馆里、还有当时仅有的几家公共图书馆里寻见的书。她每天独自待上好几个钟头，天天如此。父亲怕她孤单，常常抱回一大摞书，她便放开肚皮大嚼，在书堆里俯冲盘旋，像一位老饕面对满桌珍馐，喜得不知从哪一样下箸。就这样，十五岁上，她已读过了拜伦、简·奥斯丁、狄更斯、夏洛蒂·勃朗特（Charlotte Brontë）、费利西亚·赫曼斯（Felicia Hemans）的笔墨——更不消说E. D. E. N. 南斯沃思夫人（Mrs. E. D. E. N. Southworth）、伯莎·M. 克莱（Bertha M. Clay），还有厨房帮工的那位好仙女《炉边伴侣》（*Fireside Companion*）了——在那上头，工厂女工和公爵结起婚来，就像牛排配洋葱一样天经地义。这末一类书自然是塞莉娜那种活法的结果，从加利福尼亚到纽约，一路都是好心的房东太太、女侍和女店员借给她看的。

===Original===
Her three dark years﻿—from nine to twelve﻿—were spent with her two maiden aunts, the Misses Sarah and Abbie Peake, in the dim, prim Vermont Peake house from which her father, the black sheep, had run away when a boy. After her mother’s death Simeon Peake had sent his little daughter back east in a fit of remorse and temporary helplessness on his part and a spurt of forgiveness and churchly charity on the part of his two sisters. The two women were incredibly drawn in the pattern of the New England spinster of fiction. Mitts, preserves, Bible, chilly best room, solemn and kittenless cat, order, little-girls-mustn’t. They smelled of apples﻿—of withered apples that have rotted at the core. Selina had once found such an apple in a corner of a disorderly school-desk, had sniffed it, regarded its wrinkled, sapless pink cheek, and had bitten into it adventuresomely, only to spit out the mouthful in an explosive and unladylike spray. It had been all black and mouldy at its heart.

===Chinese===
她那三年黑暗岁月——从九岁到十二岁——是同两位未婚姑母、萨拉与艾比·皮克小姐（the Misses Sarah and Abbie Peake）一起过的，就在佛蒙特州皮克家那幢幽暗古板的宅子里——当年她父亲，家里的败家子，就是打那儿小小年纪逃出去的。母亲死后，西缅·皮克一阵悔恨、一时没了主张，把小女儿打发回东边；他的两个姐姐呢，一阵宽恕、一热乎的教会慈悲，把她收下了。这两位女人，同小说里新英格兰老处女的模子扣得一丝不差：露指手套、蜜饯果酱、《圣经》、冷冰冰的上房、一本正经而不生小猫的猫、规规矩矩，外加小姑娘家不许这样不许那样。她们身上有股苹果味——蔫苹果的味儿，烂了心的那一种。塞莉娜有一次在一张乱糟糟的课桌角落里撞见过这么一只苹果，闻了闻，端详着它那皱缩无汁的粉红脸颊，冒险家似的咬了一口，结果一口喷了出来，喷得又急又没淑女样儿。那苹果的心里全黑了，长满了霉。

===Original===
Something of this she must have conveyed, in her desperation, to her father in an uncensored letter. Without warning he had come for her, and at sight of him she had been guilty of the only fit of hysteria that marked her life, before or after the episode.

===Chinese===
情急无奈之下，她准是往一封不加检点的信里，把这份滋味多多少少写给了父亲。他毫无征兆地来接她了；一看见他，她便犯下了平生唯一一次歇斯底里——这件事之前没有过，之后也再没有过。

===Original===
So, then, from twelve to nineteen she was happy. They had come to Chicago in 1885, when she was sixteen. There they remained. Selina attended Miss Fister’s Select School for Young Ladies. When her father brought her there he had raised quite a flutter in the Fister breast﻿—so soft-spoken was he, so gentle, so sad-appearing, so winning as to smile. In the investment business, he explained. Stocks and that kind of thing. A widower. Miss Fister said, yes, she understood.

===Chinese===
于是，从十二岁到十九岁，她是快活的。1885年她十六岁，他们来到芝加哥，便在那里住了下来。塞莉娜上了菲斯特女子精英学校（Miss Fister's Select School for Young Ladies）。父亲领她去的那天，在菲斯特小姐心坎里搅起了一圈不小的涟漪——他谈吐那样温软，为人那样斯文，神色那样郁郁，笑起来又那样动人。做投资生意，他解释说。股票那一类。是一位鳏夫。菲斯特小姐说，是的，她明白。

===Original===
Simeon Peake had had nothing of the look of the professional gambler of the day. The wide slouch hat, the flowing mustache, the glittering eye, the too-bright boots, the gay cravat, all were missing in Simeon Peake’s makeup. True, he did sport a singularly clear white diamond pin in his shirt front; and his hat he wore just a little on one side. But then, these both were in the male mode and quite commonly seen. For the rest he seemed a mild and suave man, slim, a trifle diffident, speaking seldom and then with a New England drawl by which he had come honestly enough, Vermont Peake that he was.

===Chinese===
西缅·皮克浑身上下，没有一处像当时职业赌徒的做派。宽檐软呢帽、飘洒的唇髭、精光四射的眼睛、过分锃亮的靴子、花哨的领巾——这些在西缅·皮克身上一概寻不见。不错，他衬衫前襟上确确实实别着一枚异乎寻常地纯净的白钻别针，帽子也确确实实微微歪戴着。不过话说回来，这两样都还归在男人的时尚里，寻常看得见。至于其余，他看上去是个温和圆通的男子，清瘦，略带腼腆，寡言少语，开口则拖着一口新英格兰腔——这腔调他来得地道得很，谁让他生是佛蒙特皮克家的人。

===Original===
Chicago was his meat. It was booming, prosperous. Jeff Hankins’s red plush and mirrored gambling house, and Mike McDonald’s, too, both on Clark Street, knew him daily. He played in good luck and bad, but he managed somehow to see to it that there was always the money to pay for the Fister schooling. His was the ideal poker face﻿—bland, emotionless, immobile. When he was flush they ate at the Palmer House, dining off chicken or quail and thick rich soup and the apple pie for which the hostelry was famous. Waiters hovered solicitously about Simeon Peake, though he rarely addressed them and never looked at them. Selina was happy. She knew only such young people﻿—girls﻿—as she met at Miss Fister’s school. Of men, other than her father, she knew as little as a nun﻿—less. For those cloistered creatures must, if only in the conning of their Bible, learn much of the moods and passions that sway the male. The Songs of Solomon alone are a glorious sex education. But the Bible was not included in Selina’s haphazard reading, and the Gideonite was not then a force in the hotel world.

===Chinese===
芝加哥正对他的胃口。它正轰轰烈烈、欣欣向荣。克拉克街上杰夫·汉金斯（Jeff Hankins）那家红丝绒镶镜的赌场，还有迈克·麦克唐纳（Mike McDonald）的那家，都天天见他来。他手气好也上桌，手气坏也上桌，却总有法子保证菲斯特学校的学费不缺。他生就一副理想至极的扑克脸——平和、无情、纹丝不动。手头宽绰的时候，父女俩在帕尔默旅馆（Palmer House）用饭，吃鸡或者鹌鹑，喝浓汤，还有那家旅馆驰名的苹果派。侍者们围在西缅·皮克身边殷勤伺候，他却难得对他们开口，从不朝他们看一眼。塞莉娜很快活。她认识的年轻人，只有菲斯特学校里见到的那一类——全是姑娘。至于男人，父亲以外的男人，她的见识少得赛过修女——比修女还少。因为那些深居简出的人，只要翻读《圣经》，总得多多少少领教些摆布男人的脾气与情欲。单单一卷《雅歌》，就是一部堂堂皇皇的性教育读本。可《圣经》不在塞莉娜东一本西一本的阅读之列，那时候，基甸会（Gideonite）也还没在旅馆世界里成气候。

===Original===
Her chum was Julie Hempel, daughter of August Hempel, the Clark Street butcher. You probably now own some Hempel stock, if you’re lucky; and eat Hempel bacon and Hempel hams cured in the hickory, for in Chicago the distance from butcher of 1885 to packer of 1890 was only a five-year leap.

===Chinese===
她的闺蜜是朱莉·亨佩尔（Julie Hempel），克拉克街肉铺老板奥古斯特·亨佩尔（August Hempel）的女儿。运气好的话，你如今多半也攥着几股亨佩尔的股票，早餐吃的也是亨佩尔家山核桃木熏的培根和火腿——因为在芝加哥，从1885年的肉铺老板跳到1890年的肉品罐头大王，中间不过是五年轻轻一跃。

===Original===
Being so much alone developed in her a gift for the make-believe. In a comfortable, well-dressed way she was a sort of mixture of Dick Swiveller’s Marchioness and Sarah Crewe. Even in her childhood she extracted from life the double enjoyment that comes usually only to the creative mind. “Now I’m doing this. Now I’m doing that,” she told herself while she was doing it. Looking on while she participated. Perhaps her theatre-going had something to do with this. At an age when most little girls were not only unheard but practically unseen, she occupied a grown-up seat at the play, her rapt face, with its dark serious eyes, glowing in a sort of luminous pallor as she sat proudly next her father. Simeon Peake had the gambler’s love of the theatre, himself possessing the dramatic quality necessary to the successful following of his profession.

===Chinese===
长年的独处，养出了她一套假想扮演的天分。以一种舒舒服服、衣履光鲜的方式，她活脱是狄克·斯威夫勒的「侯爵夫人」（Dick Swiveller's Marchioness）和萨拉·克鲁（Sarah Crewe）的合体。早在童年，她便从生活里萃取出了双份的乐趣——那份通常只有创造的头脑才享得着的乐趣。「这会儿我在做这个。这会儿我在做那个。」她一面做，一面对自己说。一边参与，一边旁观。这或许跟她看戏有几分关系。在大多数小姑娘非但没人听见、简直没人看见的年纪，她已在戏院里坐上了成年人的座位，一张出神的小脸，配着一双严肃的黑眼睛，骄傲地挨着父亲坐，在莹白的面色里透着光。西缅·皮克怀着赌徒对戏院的那份热爱，他自己便具备着把那份营生干成功所必需的戏剧素质。

===Original===
In this way Selina, half-hidden in the depths of an orchestra seat, wriggled in ecstatic anticipation when the curtain ascended on the grotesque rows of Haverly’s minstrels. She wept (as did Simeon) over the agonies of *The Two Orphans* when Kitty Blanchard and McKee Rankin came to Chicago with the Union Square Stock Company. She witnessed that startling innovation, a Jewish play, called *Samuel of Posen*. She saw Fanny Davenport in *Pique*. Simeon even took her to a performance of that shocking and delightful form of new entertainment, the Extravaganza. She thought the plump creature in tights and spangles, descending the long stairway, the most beautiful being she had ever seen.

===Chinese===
就这样，塞莉娜半掩在乐池座位的深处，大幕升起、哈弗利黑脸吟游班（Haverly's minstrels）一排排怪诞的脸孔亮相时，她欢喜得直扭动。基蒂·布兰查德（Kitty Blanchard）和麦基·兰金（McKee Rankin）随联合广场轮演剧团（Union Square Stock Company）来芝加哥演《两个孤儿》（*The Two Orphans*）时，她（连同西缅）哭得肝肠寸断。她见识过那桩惊人的新花样——一出犹太戏，名叫《波森的塞缪尔》（*Samuel of Posen*）。她看过范妮·达文波特（Fanny Davenport）演的《皮克》（*Pique*）。西缅甚至带她去看过那种又惊世骇俗又教人心花怒放的新式娱乐——奇幻剧（Extravaganza）。她认定那个穿紧身衣裤、缀满亮片、正从长楼梯上款款而下的丰腴人儿，是她生平所见最美的造物。

===Original===
“The thing I like about plays and books is that anything can happen. Anything! You never know,” Selina said, after one of these evenings.

===Chinese===
「我喜欢戏剧和书的地方，就在于什么事都可能发生。什么事都可能！你永远猜不着。」这样一个夜晚过后，塞莉娜说。

===Original===
“No different from life,” Simeon Peake assured her. “You’ve no idea the things that happen to you if you just relax and take them as they come.”

===Chinese===
「跟生活没有两样，」西缅·皮克向她打包票，「你根本想不到，只要你放得松，事情来了接着受，会有多少事情临到你头上。」

===Original===
Curiously enough, Simeon Peake said this, not through ignorance, but deliberately and with reason. In his way and day he was a very modern father. “I want you to see all kinds,” he would say to her. “I want you to realize that this whole thing is just a grand adventure. A fine show. The trick is to play in it and look at it at the same time.”

===Chinese===
说来奇怪，西缅·皮克说这话，并非出于无知，而是有意为之、自有道理。照他的路数、他那个年代，他是个非常新派的父亲。「我要你各色人等都见识见识，」他常对她说，「我要你明白，这整个儿就是一场大冒险。一出好戏。诀窍是：身在戏中演，又同时坐在台下看。」

===Original===
“What whole thing?”

===Chinese===
「什么整个儿？」

===Original===
“Living. All mixed up. The more kinds of people you see, and the more things you do, and the more things that happen to you, the richer you are. Even if they’re not pleasant things. That’s living. Remember, no matter what happens, good or bad, it’s just so much”﻿—he used the gambler’s term, unconsciously﻿—“just so much velvet.”

===Chinese===
「活着。五花八门搅在一块儿。你见过的人越多，做过的事越多，落到你头上的事越多，你就越富有。哪怕不是痛快事，也一样。那才叫活着。记住喽，不管发生什么，好事坏事，统统不过是」——他无意识地用了句赌徒行话——「统统不过是白饶的外快。」

===Original===
But Selina, somehow, understood. “You mean that anything’s better than being Aunt Sarah and Aunt Abbie.”

===Chinese===
可塞莉娜不知怎么就懂了。「你是说，什么都比当萨拉姑姑和艾比姑姑强。」

===Original===
“Well﻿—yes. There are only two kinds of people in the world that really count. One kind’s wheat and the other kind’s emeralds.”

===Chinese===
「唔——对。这世上真正算数的，只有两种人。一种是麦子，另一种是绿宝石。」

===Original===
“Fanny Davenport’s an emerald,” said Selina, quickly, and rather surprised to find herself saying it.

===Chinese===
「范妮·达文波特是绿宝石。」塞莉娜说得飞快，自己也吃一惊，怎么会脱口说出这个来。

===Original===
“Yes. That’s it.”

===Chinese===
「对。就是这个理。」

===Original===
“And﻿—and Julie Hempel’s father﻿—he’s wheat.”

===Chinese===
「那——那朱莉·亨佩尔的爸爸——他是麦子。」

===Original===
“By golly, Sele!” shouted Simeon Peake. “You’re a shrewd little tyke!”

===Chinese===
「好家伙，塞儿！」西缅·皮克嚷道。「你这个小机灵鬼！」

===Original===
It was after reading *Pride and Prejudice* that she decided to be the Jane Austen of her time. She became very mysterious and enjoyed a brief period of unpopularity at Miss Fister’s owing to her veiled allusions to her “work”; and an annoying way of smiling to herself and tapping a ruminative toe as though engaged in visions far too exquisite for the common eye. Her chum Julie Hempel, properly enough, became enraged at this and gave Selina to understand that she must make her choice between revealing her secret or being cast out of the Hempel heart. Selina swore her to secrecy.

===Chinese===
读了《傲慢与偏见》（*Pride and Prejudice*）之后，她决意要做她那个时代的简·奥斯丁。她变得讳莫如深，在菲斯特学校着实过了一小段无人搭理的日子——因为她影影绰绰地总提自己的「作品」，又老是一个人莞尔、若有所思地拿脚尖点地，仿佛沉浸在凡胎肉眼看不得的美景里。她的闺蜜朱莉·亨佩尔呢，气得恰到好处，正告塞莉娜：要么吐露秘密，要么被逐出亨佩尔家的心，二者必居其一。塞莉娜先勒令她赌咒保密。

===Original===
“Very well, then. Now I’ll tell you. I’m going to be a novelist.” Julie was palpably disappointed, though she said, “Selina!” as though properly impressed, but followed it up with: “Still, I don’t see why you had to be so mysterious about it.”

===Chinese===
「那好。现在我可以告诉你了。我要当个小说家。」朱莉那份失望明明白白挂在脸上，嘴上却说：「塞莉娜！」一副被震住了的样子，紧跟着又追一句：「不过我看不出，你有什么必要搞得这么神神秘秘的。」

===Original===
“You just don’t understand, Julie. Writers have to study life at first hand. And if people know you’re studying them they don’t act natural. Now, that day you were telling me about the young man in your father’s shop who looked at you and said﻿—”

===Chinese===
「你就是不懂，朱莉。作家必须到生活现场去学。人家要是知道你在观察他们，就不自然了。就说那天吧，你跟我讲你们铺子里那个小伙子，他瞧着你，说——」

===Original===
“Selina Peake, if you dare to put that in your book I’ll never speak﻿—”

===Chinese===
「塞莉娜·皮克，你敢把这段写进书里，我就再也不跟你——」

===Original===
“All right. I won’t. But that’s what I mean. You see!”

===Chinese===
「好啦，不写。可我就是这个意思。你瞧见了吧！」

===Original===
Julie Hempel and Selina Peake, both finished products of Miss Fister’s school, were of an age﻿—nineteen. Selina, on this September day, had been spending the afternoon with Julie, and now, adjusting her hat preparatory to leaving, she clapped her hands over her ears to shut out the sounds of Julie’s importunings that she stay to supper. Certainly the prospect of the usual Monday evening meal in Mrs. Tebbitt’s boarding house (the Peake luck was momentarily low) did not present sufficient excuse for Selina’s refusal. Indeed, the Hempel supper as sketched dish for dish by the urgent Julie brought little greedy groans from Selina.

===Chinese===
朱莉·亨佩尔和塞莉娜·皮克，同是菲斯特学校的出品，两人同岁——十九。这个九月的下午，塞莉娜一直在朱莉家消磨，这会儿正戴帽子准备动身，朱莉连声苦留她吃晚饭，她直拿手捂耳朵。当然，眼看特比特太太（Mrs. Tebbitt）包伙公寓里那顿照例的星期一晚饭（皮克家的手头正一时拮据），实在成不了塞莉娜推辞的充分理由。说实在的，急切的朱莉一道菜一道菜报出来的亨佩尔家晚饭，馋得塞莉娜直发出一声声小小的、贪婪的呻吟。

===Original===
“It’s prairie chickens﻿—three of them﻿—that a farmer west of town brought Father. Mother fixes them with stuffing, and there’s currant jell. Creamed onions and baked tomatoes. And for dessert, apple roll.”

===Chinese===
「有草原松鸡——三只呢——城西一个农民送给爸爸的。妈妈要塞上填料烤，还有醋栗冻。奶油汁洋葱、烤番茄。甜点是苹果卷儿。」

===Original===
Selina snapped the elastic holding her high-crowned hat under her chignon of hair in the back. She uttered a final and quavering groan. “On Monday nights we have cold mutton and cabbage at Mrs. Tebbitt’s. This is Monday.”

===Chinese===
塞莉娜把那顶高顶帽的帽带啪的一声扣到脑后的发髻底下，发出最后一声颤巍巍的馋呻吟。「星期一晚上，特比特太太家吃冷羊肉煮卷心菜。今天就是星期一。」

===Original===
“Well then, silly, why not stay!”

===Chinese===
「那你还犟什么，傻丫头，留下呀！」

===Original===
“Father comes home at six. If I’m not there he’s disappointed.”

===Chinese===
「爸爸六点回来。我要不在家，他会失望的。」

===Original===
Julie, plump, blonde, placid, forsook her soft white blandishments and tried steel against the steel of Selina’s decision.

===Chinese===
丰润、金发、性子恬静的朱莉，撇开了她那套温软白嫩的哄劝，改用钢来对付塞莉娜主意里的钢。

===Original===
“He leaves you right after supper. And you’re alone every night until twelve and after.”

===Chinese===
「他一吃完晚饭就走。往后你夜夜一个人待到十二点，还更晚。」

===Original===
“I don’t see what that has to do with it,” Selina said, stiffly.

===Chinese===
「我看不出这跟那有什么相干。」塞莉娜硬邦邦地说。

===Original===
Julie’s steel, being low-grade, melted at once and ran off her in rivulets. “Of course it hasn’t, Selie dear. Only I thought you might leave him just this once.”

===Chinese===
朱莉那点钢原是劣质货，登时化了，顺着她身上淌成一道道细流。「当然不相干，塞莉亲爱的。我只是想，就这一回，你兴许可以撇下他。」

===Original===
“If I’m not there he’s disappointed. And that terrible Mrs. Tebbitt makes eyes at him. He hates it there.”

===Chinese===
「我要不在家，他会失望的。再说那个可怕的特比特太太老冲他挤眉弄眼。他讨厌那地方。」

===Original===
“Then I don’t see why you stay. I never could see. You’ve been there four months now, and I think it’s horrid and stuffy; and oilcloth on the stairs.”

===Chinese===
「那我就不明白你干吗还住那儿了。我一直就不明白。你们都住了四个月了，我看那地方又糟糕又憋闷，楼梯上还铺着油布。」

===Original===
“Father has had some temporary business setbacks.”

===Chinese===
「爸爸生意上碰了点暂时的挫折。」

===Original===
Selina’s costume testified to that. True, it was modish, and bustled, and basqued, and flounced; and her high-crowned, short-rimmed hat, with its trimming of feathers and flowers and ribbons had come from New York. But both were of last spring’s purchasing, and this was September.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜这一身行头便是明证。不错，它入时，撑着腰垫，束着卡腰的紧身，缀着层层褶裥；那顶高顶短檐帽，缀着羽毛、花朵和缎带，是从纽约买来的。可这两样都是去年春天置办下的，而如今已是九月。

===Original===
In the course of the afternoon they had been looking over the pages of *Godey’s Ladies’ Book* for that month. The disparity between Selina’s costume and the creations pictured there was much as the difference between the Tebbitt meal and that outlined by Julie. Now Julie, fond though defeated, kissed her friend goodbye.

===Chinese===
那天下午，她们一直在翻当月的《戈迪女士杂志》（*Godey's Ladies' Book*）。塞莉娜这身衣裳和画页上那些时新创制之间的悬殊，同特比特家的饭食与朱莉报出的菜单之间的悬殊，也大致相当。这会儿，宠爱着却打了败仗的朱莉吻别了她的朋友。

===Original===
Selina walked quickly the short distance from the Hempel house to Tebbitt’s, on Dearborn Avenue. Up in her second-floor room she took off her hat and called to her father, but he had not yet come in. She was glad of that. She had been fearful of being late. She regarded her hat now with some distaste, decided to rip off the faded spring roses, did rip a stitch or two, only to discover that the hat material was more faded than the roses, and that the uncovered surface showed up a dark splotch like a wall-spot when a picture, long hung, is removed. So she got a needle and prepared to tack the offending rose in its accustomed place.

===Chinese===
从亨佩尔家到迪尔伯恩大道（Dearborn Avenue）的特比特家只有短短一段路，塞莉娜走得很快。上楼进了自己二层的房间，她摘下帽子，喊了声父亲，人还没回来。她倒高兴。她方才一直怕自己回来晚了呢。这会儿她带着几分嫌弃端详那顶帽子，决意把那几朵褪色的春日绢玫瑰拆下来，也真拆了一两针，却发现帽子料子比玫瑰花儿褪得还厉害，花底下露出的那块，显出一圈深色印子，就像挂了多年画的墙上，画一摘下来便留下的那块印。于是她取来针，预备把那几朵惹是生非的玫瑰重新缀回老地方。

===Original===
Perched on the arm of a chair near the window, taking quick deft stitches, she heard a sound. She had never heard that sound before﻿—that peculiar sound﻿—the slow, ominous tread of men laden with a heavy inert burden; bearing with infinite care that which was well beyond hurting. Selina had never heard that sound before, and yet, hearing it, she recognized it by one of those pangs, centuries old, called woman’s instinct. Thud﻿—shuffle﻿—thud﻿—shuffle﻿—up the narrow stairway, along the passage. She stood up, the needle poised in her hand. The hat fell to the floor. Her eyes were wide, fixed. Her lips slightly parted. The listening look. She knew.

===Chinese===
她倚在窗口椅子的扶手上飞针走线，忽然听见一种声响。她从未听过那种声响——那种古怪的声响——一群男人抬着一件沉死呆重的担子，缓慢地、不祥地踏着步子；怀着万般的小心，抬着一件早已不会再疼的东西。塞莉娜从未听过这种声响，然而一听见，她便认出了它，凭的是那记千百年的刺痛——世称女人直觉。咚——嚓——咚——嚓——上了窄窄的楼梯，穿过过道。她站起身，针尖还悬在指间。帽子掉在了地上。她双眼睁大，直定定的。嘴唇微微张开。一副谛听的神情。她知道了。

===Original===
She knew even before she heard the hoarse man’s voice saying, “Lift ’er up there a little on the corner, now. Easy﻿—e-e-easy.” And Mrs. Tebbitt’s high shrill clamour: “You can’t bring it in there! You hadn’t ought to bring it in here like this!”

===Chinese===
甚至不等那个沙哑的男人嗓音响起来，她就已经知道了：「角上那儿再往上抬一点，喏。轻——点——轻——点——」还有特比特太太尖利刺耳的吵嚷：「你们不能把他抬到那儿去！你们不能就这样把他往这儿抬！」

===Original===
Selina’s suspended breath came back. She was panting now. She had flung open the door. A flat still burden partially covered with an overcoat carelessly flung over the face. The feet, in their square-toed boots, wobbled listlessly. Selina noticed how shiny the boots were. He was always very finicking about such things.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜屏着的那口气缓了回来。她直喘。她已经冲开了房门。一副平直僵卧的担子，一件随手抛上去的大衣半掩着那张脸。方头靴子里的两只脚，有气无力地晃荡。塞莉娜注意到那双靴子多亮。他对这些向来讲究得很。

===Original===
Simeon Peake had been shot in Jeff Hankins’s place at five in the afternoon. The irony of it was that the bullet had not been intended for him at all. Its derelict course had been due to feminine aim. Sped by one of those over-dramatic ladies who, armed with horsewhip or pistol in tardy defence of their honour, spangled Chicago’s dull ’80s with their doings, it had been meant for a well-known newspaper publisher usually mentioned (in papers other than his own) as a bon vivant. The lady’s leaden remonstrance was to have been proof of the fact that he had been more vivacious than bon.

===Chinese===
西缅·皮克是在杰夫·汉金斯那家赌场里挨的枪，下午五点钟。造化弄人之处在于，那颗子弹压根儿不是冲他来的。它偏出的弹道，得归咎于一位女士的准头。开枪的是那类过分戏剧化的女士之一——她们手擎马鞭或手枪，姗姗来迟地捍卫自己的名誉，她们的事迹给芝加哥沉闷的80年代缀上了亮片——子弹本是赏给一位大名鼎鼎的报馆老板的，此人（在他自家以外的报纸上）一贯被唤作美食家（bon vivant）。那位女士这番铅弹的抗议，原是要证明他在这名号里头，「作乐」占得多，「美食」占得少。

===Original===
It was, perhaps, because of this that the matter was pretty well hushed up. The publisher’s paper﻿—which was Chicago’s foremost﻿—scarcely mentioned the incident and purposely misspelled the name. The lady, thinking her task accomplished, had taken truer aim with her second bullet, and had saved herself the trouble of trial by human jury.

===Chinese===
或许正因如此，这件事给捂了个严实。那位老板的报纸——芝加哥的头号大报——对这件事几乎只字不提，还故意把名字拼错。那位女士自认大功告成，又用第二颗子弹瞄得实实在在地打了自己，省去了受人间陪审团审判的麻烦。

===Original===
Simeon Peake left his daughter Selina a legacy of two fine clear blue-white diamonds (he had had the gambler’s love of them) and the sum of four hundred and ninety-seven dollars in cash. Just how he had managed to have a sum like this put by was a mystery. The envelope containing it had evidently once held a larger sum. It had been sealed, and then slit. On the outside was written, in Simeon Peake’s fine, almost feminine hand: “For my little daughter Selina Peake in case anything should happen to me.” It bore a date seven years old. What the original sum had been no one ever knew. That any sum remained was evidence of the almost heroic self-control practised by one to whom money﻿—ready money in any sum at all﻿—meant only fuel to feed the flames of his gaming fever.

===Chinese===
西缅·皮克留给女儿塞莉娜的遗产，是两颗上等纯净的蓝白钻石（他怀着赌徒对钻石的那份爱好），和四百九十七块现钱。他是怎么想法子攒下这么一笔钱的，是个谜。装钱的信封分明装过更大的数目。封过口，又裁开了。外面用西缅·皮克那笔秀气的、近乎女人的字写着：「给我的小女儿塞莉娜·皮克，以防我有什么不测。」落的是七年前的日期。原先那笔钱有多少，谁也没知道过。居然还剩下这一笔，便足以证明他那份近乎豪杰的自制——对这样一个人，钱——任何数目的现钱——不过是往赌瘾的火苗上添的柴。

===Original===
To Selina fell the choice of earning her own living or of returning to the Vermont village and becoming a withered and sapless dried apple, with black fuzz and mould at her heart, like her aunts, the Misses Sarah and Abbie Peake. She did not hesitate.

===Chinese===
摆在塞莉娜面前的选择是：要么自食其力，要么回佛蒙特的村子去，做一只蔫干瘪、心里长了黑绒毛和霉的苹果，像她那两位姑母、萨拉与艾比·皮克小姐一样。她没有半点犹豫。

===Original===
“But what kind of work?” Julie Hempel demanded. “What kind of work can you do?” Women﻿—that is, the Selina Peakes﻿—did not work.

===Chinese===
「可是，做什么工作呢？」朱莉·亨佩尔诘问。「你能做什么工作？」女人家——也就是说，塞莉娜·皮克这样的女人家——是不做工的。

===Original===
“I﻿—well, I can teach.”

===Chinese===
「我——呃，我可以教书。」

===Original===
“Teach what?”

===Chinese===
「教什么？」

===Original===
“The things I learned at Miss Fister’s.”

===Chinese===
「教我在菲斯特学校学的那几样呗。」

===Original===
Julie’s expression weighed and discredited Miss Fister. “Who to?” Which certainly justified her expression.

===Chinese===
朱莉脸上的神情把菲斯特学校掂量了一番，又作践了一番。「教谁呢？」这一问，倒确乎对得起她那副神情了。

===Original===
“To children. People’s children. Or in the public schools.”

===Chinese===
「教孩子。人家们的孩子。要不就上公立学校。」

===Original===
“You have to do something first﻿—go to Normal, or teach in the country, don’t you?﻿—before you can teach in the public schools. They’re mostly old. Twenty-five or even thirty﻿—or more!” with nineteen’s incapacity to imagine an age beyond thirty.

===Chinese===
「那总得先干点别的吧——先上师范，要么先在乡下教书，不是吗？——然后才进得了公立学校。那里头的教师多半都老了。二十五岁，甚至三十岁——还往上了呢！」说这话，带着十九岁的人对三十开外年纪的全然无法想象。

===Original===
That Julie was taking the offensive in this conversation, and Selina the defensive, was indicative of the girl’s numbed state. Selina did not then know the iron qualities her friend was displaying in being with her at all. Mrs. Hempel had quite properly forbidden Julie ever to see the dead dissolute gambler’s daughter again. She had even sent a note to Miss Fister expressing her opinion of a school which would, by admitting such unselected ladies to its select circle, expose other pupils to contamination.

===Chinese===
这一场对话里，朱莉攻，塞莉娜守——足见这姑娘已经木了。塞莉娜那时还不知道，她朋友光是这样陪在她身边，就在显一副铁打的心肠。亨佩尔太太早就有板有眼地禁止朱莉再见这个死掉的荒唐赌徒的女儿，甚至给菲斯特小姐递过一张条子，表明她对这所学校的看法：这样一所学校，竟容许这等未经筛选的女士混进精选的圈子，教别的学生蒙受沾染。

===Original===
Selina rallied to Julie’s onslaught. “Then I’ll just teach a country school. I’m good at arithmetic. You know that.” Julie should have known it, having had all her Fister sums solved by Selina. “Country schools are just arithmetic and grammar and geography.”

===Chinese===
塞莉娜振作起来，迎击朱莉的猛攻。「那我就去教乡村学校。我算术好。这你知道。」朱莉理应知道——她那些菲斯特算术题，向来都是塞莉娜给解的。「乡村学校嘛，不外乎算术、语法、地理。」

===Original===
“You! Teaching a country school!”

===Chinese===
「你！去教乡村学校！」

===Original===
She looked at Selina.

===Chinese===
她看着塞莉娜。

===Original===
She saw a misleadingly delicate face, the skull small and exquisitely formed. The cheek bones rather high﻿—or perhaps they looked so because of the fact that the eyes, dark, soft, and luminous, were unusually deep-set in their sockets. The face, instead of narrowing to a soft curve at the chin, developed unexpected strength in the jaw line. That line, fine, steel-strong, sharp and clear, was of the stuff of which pioneer women are made. Julie, inexperienced in the art of reading the human physiognomy, did not decipher the meaning of it. Selina’s hair was thick, long, and fine, so that she piled it easily in the loops, coils, and knots that fashion demanded. Her nose, slightly pinched at the nostrils, was exquisite. When she laughed it had the trick of wrinkling just a little across the narrow bridge; very engaging, and mischievous. She was thought a rather plain little thing, which she wasn’t. But the eyes were what you marked and remembered. People to whom she was speaking had a way of looking into them deeply. Selina was often embarrassed to discover that they were not hearing what she had to say. Perhaps it was this velvety softness of the eyes that caused one to overlook the firmness of the lower face. When the next ten years had done their worst to her, and Julie had suddenly come upon her stepping agilely out of a truck gardener’s wagon on Prairie Avenue, a tanned, weather-beaten, toil-worn woman, her abundant hair skewered into a knob and held by a long gray hairpin, her full calico skirt grimed with the mud of the wagon wheel, a pair of men’s old side-boots on her slim feet, a grotesquely battered old felt hat (her husband’s) on her head, her arms full of ears of sweet corn, and carrots, and radishes, and bunches of beets; a woman with bad teeth, flat breasts, a sagging pocket in her capacious skirt﻿—even then Julie, staring, had known her by her eyes. And she had run to her in her silk suit and her fine silk shirtwaist and her hat with the plume and had cried, “Oh, Selina! My dear! My dear!”﻿—with a sob of horror and pity﻿—“My dear.” And had taken Selina, carrots, beets, corn, and radishes, in her arms. The vegetables lay scattered all about them on the sidewalk in front of Julie Hempel Arnold’s great stone house on Prairie Avenue. But strangely enough it had been Selina who had done the comforting, patting Julie’s silken shoulder and saying, over and over, “There, there! It’s all right, Julie. It’s all right. Don’t cry. What’s there to cry for! Sh!﻿ ﻿… It’s all right.”

===Chinese===
她看见一张看似纤弱的脸，头骨小巧，样样精致。颧骨略高了些——也许只是因为那双又黑又软、莹莹有光的眼睛在眼窝里陷得格外深，才显得如此。这张脸到了下巴处不收成一道柔和的圆弧，倒在下颌线上生出意外的力量。那道线，细而如钢，锋利分明，正是铸成拓荒女人的那号材料。朱莉不谙相面之术，解不出这条线的意思。塞莉娜的头发又厚又长又细软，时装兴什么环、圈、髻，她随手便挽得起。她的鼻子，鼻翼微微内收，精致极了。她一笑，窄窄的鼻梁上便微微起一道横褶，非常动人，也非常淘气。人家都当她是个不起眼的小东西，其实不然。可真正教你过目难忘的，是那双眼睛。同她说话的人，总不由自主往那对眼睛深处望。塞莉娜常常窘迫地发现，人家压根儿没在听她讲的话。也许正是这双眼睛丝绒般的软，教人看漏了下半张脸的硬。等往后的十年把她作践到了头，有一天朱莉猛不丁撞见她从一辆菜农的马车上灵便地跳下来，就在草原大道（Prairie Avenue）上——一个晒得黢黑、饱经风霜、操劳过度的女人，一头浓发胡乱绾成个髻，别一根长长的灰发卡，宽大的印花布裙糊满车轮溅的泥，一双秀气的脚上套着男人的旧靴子，头上扣一顶瘪得奇形怪状的旧毡帽（她丈夫的），怀里抱满了甜玉米穗、胡萝卜、小萝卜，还有一把把甜菜；一个牙齿坏了、胸脯瘪了、宽大裙腰里坠着只松塌口袋的女人——即便在那时，朱莉瞪着她，也还是凭那双眼睛认出了她。于是她穿着真丝套裙、精美的真丝衬衫、插着羽毛的帽子奔了过去，喊道：「哦，塞莉娜！我亲爱的！我亲爱的！」——带着一声惊骇又怜惜的抽噎——「我亲爱的。」她把塞莉娜连同胡萝卜、甜菜、玉米和小萝卜一把搂进怀里。蔬菜滚散了一地，撒在两人脚边的人行道上，就在朱莉·亨佩尔·阿诺德（Julie Hempel Arnold）草原大道上那座石头大宅的前面。可是说来奇怪，反倒一直是塞莉娜在安慰人，轻拍着朱莉丝绸的肩头，一遍又一遍地说：「好了，好了！没事的，朱莉。没事的。别哭了。有什么可哭的呢！嘘！……没事的。」

## II / 第二章

===Original===
Selina had thought herself lucky to get the Dutch school at High Prairie, ten miles outside Chicago. Thirty dollars a month! She was to board at the house of Klaas Pool, the truck farmer. It was August Hempel who had brought it all about; or Julie, urging him. Now, at forty-five, August Hempel, the Clark Street butcher, knew every farmer and stockman for miles around, and hundreds besides scattered throughout Cook County and the State of Illinois.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜觉得自己能谋到高草原那所荷兰人学校，实在是运气——那地方在芝加哥城外十里。一个月三十美元！她将寄宿在蔬菜农场主克拉斯·普尔（Klaas Pool）家里。促成这一切的是奥古斯特·亨佩尔；或者不如说，是撺掇着他的朱莉。如今四十五岁的奥古斯特·亨佩尔，克拉克街的肉铺老板，方圆多少里的农户和牲口贩子没有一个他不认得，此外还有数以百计的人，散布在库克县（Cook County）和伊利诺伊州全境。

===Original===
To get the Dutch school for Selina Peake was a simple enough matter for him. The High Prairie district school teacher had always, heretofore, been a man. A more advantageous position presenting itself, this year’s prospective teacher had withdrawn before the school term had begun. This was in September. High Prairie school did not open until the first week in November. In that region of truck farms every boy and girl over six was busy in the fields throughout the early autumn. Two years of this, and Selina would be qualified for a city grade. August Hempel indicated that he could arrange that, too, when the time came. Selina thought this shrewd red-faced butcher a wonderful man, indeed. Which he was.

===Chinese===
替塞莉娜·皮克谋下这所荷兰人学校，在他不过是举手之劳。高草原学区的教师，此前向来用的是男人。今年原先聘定的教师另谋到一处更有油水的位置，学期还没开学便辞了约。这是九月里的事。高草原的学校要到十一月第一个星期才开课。在那片蔬菜农场连绵的地面上，一到早秋，凡是满了六岁的男孩女孩，个个都要下地忙活。这样教上两年，塞莉娜就能取得城里学校的任教资格。奥古斯特·亨佩尔示意，到时这一层他也可以打点。塞莉娜心想，这位精明的红脸膛肉铺老板，可真是个了不起的人物。他也确实了不起。

===Original===
At forty-seven, single-handed, he was to establish the famous Hempel Packing Company. At fifty he was the power in the yards, and there were Hempel branches in Kansas City, Omaha, Denver. At sixty you saw the name of Hempel plastered over packing sheds, factories, and canning plants all the way from Honolulu to Portland. You read:

===Chinese===
四十七岁上，他单枪匹马，办起了赫赫有名的亨佩尔肉品加工公司（Hempel Packing Company）。五十岁上，他成了大围场里说一不二的人物，堪萨斯城、奥马哈、丹佛都开了亨佩尔的分号。六十岁上，从檀香山到波特兰，屠宰棚、工厂、罐头厂，处处刷着亨佩尔的名字。你读到：

===Original===
Don’t Say Ham: Say Hempel’s.

===Chinese===
别说火腿：要说亨佩尔的。

===Original===
Hempel products ranged incredibly from pork to pineapple; from grease to grape-juice. An indictment meant no more to Hempel, the packer, than an injunction for speeding to you. Something of his character may be gleaned from the fact that farmers who had known the butcher at forty still addressed this millionaire, at sixty, as Aug. At sixty-five he took up golf and beat his son-in-law, Michael Arnold, at it. A magnificent old pirate, sailing the perilous commercial seas of the American ’90s before commissions, investigations, and inquisitive senate insisted on applying whitewash to the black flag of trade.

===Chinese===
亨佩尔家的产品，经营范围之广令人咋舌：从猪肉到菠萝，从油脂到葡萄汁。一纸起诉书，在加工商亨佩尔那里，同你接到一张因超速而下的禁制令差不多，算不得什么。他的为人，从一件事上便可略见一斑：那些在他四十岁上就认得这位肉铺老板的农户，到他六十岁成了百万富翁，照旧管他叫「奥格」（Aug）。六十五岁上他学打高尔夫，把女婿迈克尔·阿诺德（Michael Arnold）打得落花流水。好一位气派堂堂的老海盗，扬帆于美国 90 年代那片凶险的商海——那时节，形形色色的委员会、调查、爱刨根问底的参议院，还没有硬逼着人往贸易的黑旗上刷白灰。

===Original===
Selina went about her preparations in a singularly clearheaded fashion, considering her youth and inexperience. She sold one of the blue-white diamonds, and kept one. She placed her inheritance of four hundred and ninety-seven dollars, complete, in the bank. She bought stout sensible boots, two dresses, one a brown lady’s-cloth which she made herself, finished with white collars and cuffs, very neat (the cuffs to be protected by black sateen sleevelets, of course, while teaching); and a wine-red cashmere (mad, but she couldn’t resist it) for best.

===Chinese===
以她的年轻、她的没阅历而论，塞莉娜打点行装的方式，头脑清醒得出奇。她卖掉一颗蓝白钻，留下一颗。她把四百九十七美元的遗产一文不动，尽数存进了银行。她买了结实耐穿的靴子，两身衣裙：一身棕色女装呢的，自己裁自己缝，缀上白领子白袖口，十分齐整（教书时袖口自然要用黑棉缎袖套保护着）；另一身酒红色开司米（昏了头，可她实在抗拒不了），留着出客穿。

===Original===
She eagerly learned what she could of this region once known as New Holland. Its people were all truck gardeners, and as Dutch as the Netherlands from which they or their fathers had come. She heard stories of wooden shoes worn in the wet prairie fields; of a red-faced plodding Cornelius Van der Bilt living in placid ignorance of the existence of his distinguished New York patronymic connection; of sturdy, phlegmatic, industrious farmers in squat, many-windowed houses patterned after the north Holland houses of their European memories. Many of them had come from the town of Schoorl, or near it. Others from the lowlands outside Amsterdam. Selina pictured it another Sleepy Hollow, a replica of the quaint settlement in Washington Irving’s delightful tale. The deserting schoolmaster had been a second Ichabod Crane, naturally; the farmer at whose house she was to live a modern Mynheer Van Tassel, pipe, chuckle, and all. She and Julie Hempel read the tale over together on an afternoon when Julie managed to evade the maternal edict. Selina, picturing mellow golden corn fields; crusty crullers, crumbling oly-koeks, toothsome wild ducks, sides of smoked beef, pumpkin pies; country dances, apple-cheeked farmer girls, felt sorry for poor Julie staying on in the dull gray commonplaceness of Chicago.

===Chinese===
这片旧称新荷兰的地方，凡是能打听的，塞莉娜都急切地去学。那里的居民全是种菜的园匠，个个是道地的荷兰人，同他们本人或父辈所来自的尼德兰一样道地。她听人讲起种种故事：湿漉漉的草原田里脚蹬木鞋；有个红脸膛、一根筋埋头苦干的科尼利厄斯·范德比尔特（Cornelius Van der Bilt），对自家姓氏在纽约的那门显赫本家浑然不觉，日子过得安然自在；还有那些结实、木讷、勤勉的农户，住在低矮多窗的房子里，式样照着他们欧洲记忆里荷兰北部的房子。他们许多人是斯库尔（Schoorl）镇或那一带来的，另一些人来自阿姆斯特丹城外的低地。塞莉娜把这里想象成又一座睡谷（Sleepy Hollow），华盛顿·欧文（Washington Irving）那篇妙趣横生的故事里那座古雅村落的翻版。那个弃职而逃的教书匠，自然便是第二个伊卡博德·克莱恩（Ichabod Crane）；她要去寄宿的那家农户的主人，则是一位现代的范·塔塞尔老爷（Mynheer Van Tassel，Mynheer 是荷兰语的「老爷」）——烟斗、呵呵一笑，一样不缺。赶着一个下午，朱莉·亨佩尔设法避开了母亲的禁令，两人便凑在一处，把那篇故事从头重温了一遍。塞莉娜想象着金黄醇熟的玉米地；脆皮的炸麻花、酥松的荷兰油团子（oly-koeks，荷兰语「油糕」，一种油炸甜面团）、肥美的野鸭、一方方熏牛肉、南瓜派；乡村舞会，苹果红脸蛋的农家姑娘——她不由得替可怜的朱莉难过，怪她偏要守着芝加哥那灰扑扑的平淡乏味。

===Original===
The last week in October found her on the way to High Prairie, seated beside Klaas Pool in the two-horse wagon with which he brought his garden stuff to the Chicago market. She sat perched next him on the high seat like a saucy wren beside a ruminant Holstein. So they jolted up the long Halsted road through the late October sunset. The prairie land just outside Chicago had not then been made a terrifying and epic thing of slagheaps, smokestacks, and blast furnaces like a Pennell drawing. Today it stretched away and away in the last rays of the late autumn sunlight over which the lake mist was beginning to creep like chiffon covering gold. Mile after mile of cabbage fields, jade-green against the earth. Mile after mile of red cabbage, a rich plummy Burgundy veined with black. Between these, heaps of corn were piled-up sunshine. Against the horizon an occasional patch of woods showed the last russet and bronze of oak and maple. These things Selina saw with her beauty-loving eye, and she clasped her hands in their black cotton gloves.

===Chinese===
十月最后一个星期，她已在去高草原的路上，坐在克拉斯·普尔身旁的双马板车里——他平日就是赶着这辆车，把园子里的出产拉到芝加哥市场去卖的。她栖在他旁边的高车座上，活像一只泼辣的小鹪鹩偎着一头反刍的荷斯坦牛。两人就这样一路颠簸，沿着长长的哈尔斯特德路（Halsted），行在十月下旬的落日里。芝加哥城外的这片草原，那时还没有变成炉渣堆、大烟囱、高炉凑成的骇人的史诗图景，不像彭内尔（Pennell）的画。这一天，它在深秋阳光最后的余晖里绵延复绵延，湖上的雾开始悄悄爬拢过来，像雪纺纱蒙上金子。一英里又一英里的卷心菜田，玉绿色衬着泥土。一英里又一英里的紫红甘蓝，浓郁的梅子色勃艮第，脉纹发黑。两者之间，一堆堆玉米是码起来的阳光。天边偶尔一小片树林，显出橡树枫树最后的黄褐与青铜。这一切，塞莉娜用她那双爱美的眼睛尽收眼底，她那戴黑棉手套的双手不禁紧紧攥在了一起。

===Original===
“Oh, Mr. Pool!” she cried. “Mr. Pool! How beautiful it is here!”

===Chinese===
「哦，普尔先生！」她喊道。「普尔先生！这儿多美呀！」

===Original===
Klaas Pool, driving his team of horses down the muddy Halsted road, was looking straight ahead, his eyes fastened seemingly on an invisible spot between the off-horse’s ears. His was not the kind of brain that acts quickly, nor was his body’s mechanism the sort that quickly responds to that brain’s message. His eyes were china-blue in a round red face that was covered with a stubble of stiff golden hairs. His round moon of a head was set low and solidly between his great shoulders, so that as he began to turn it now, slowly, you marvelled at the process and waited fearfully to hear a creak. He was turning his head toward Selina, but keeping his gaze on the spot between his horse’s ears. Evidently the head and the eyes revolved by quite distinct processes. Now he faced Selina almost directly. Then he brought his eyes around, slowly, until they focused on her cameo-like face all alight now with her enjoyment of the scene around her; with a certain elation at this new venture into which she was entering; and with excitement such as she used to feel when the curtain rose with tantalizing deliberateness on the first act of a play which she was seeing with her father. She was well bundled up against the sharp October air in her cloak and muffler, with a shawl tucked about her knees and waist. The usual creamy pallor of her fine clear skin showed an unwonted pink, and her eyes were wide, dark, and bright. Beside this sparkling delicate girl’s face Klaas Pool’s heavy features seemed carved from the stuff of another clay and race. His pale blue eyes showed incomprehension.

===Chinese===
克拉斯·普尔赶着他的两匹马，行在泥泞的哈尔斯特德路上，两眼直视前方，目光像是钉在右首那匹马的耳朵之间一个看不见的点上。他的脑子不是那种说动就动的脑子，他身上的机括，也不是那种一接到脑子的信儿就立刻照办的机括。他一双瓷蓝色的眼睛，嵌在一张滚圆的红脸膛上，脸上覆着一层硬扎扎的金色胡茬。他那颗圆月似的脑袋，低而结实地安在两大坨肩膀之间，因此这会儿他开始转过头来——慢慢地——你会惊叹这道工序，还提心吊胆地等着听见一声吱呀。他正把头朝塞莉娜转过来，目光却还留在马耳朵之间那个点上。显然，脑袋和眼睛是按两套全然不同的程序各自旋转的。这会儿他差不多正对着塞莉娜了。然后他把眼睛也挪过来，慢慢地，直到焦点落在她那张浮雕宝石似的脸上——那张脸此刻焕然发亮，为她周遭这片景致而喜，为她即将投身其中的这桩新事业而隐隐振奋，还带着一种她从前随父亲看戏、大幕存心吊人胃口地缓缓升起、第一幕将启时的那种兴奋。她裹得严严实实，抵御十月凛冽的空气：斗篷、围巾，膝上腰间还掖着一条披肩。她那细净皮肤惯常的乳白，此刻透出一层难得的粉红，眼睛睁得大大的，又黑又亮。在这张光彩流转、娇柔纤秀的姑娘脸旁边，克拉斯·普尔那副粗重的五官，仿佛是拿另一种泥、另一个族类的料子雕出来的。他淡蓝的眼睛里一片茫然。

===Original===
“Beautiful?” he echoed, in puzzled interrogation. “What is beautiful?”

===Chinese===
「美？」他应了一声，是大惑不解的诘问。「什么美？」

===Original===
Selina’s slim arms flashed out from the swathings of cloak, shawl, and muffler and were flung wide in a gesture that embraced the landscape on which the late afternoon sun was casting a glow peculiar to that lake region, all rose and golden and mist-shimmering.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜那双纤细的胳膊从斗篷、披肩、围巾的重重包裹里倏地闪出来，猛地张开，做了一个把这整片风景都揽进怀里的姿势——午后的太阳正给这片风景镀上一层那湖区特有的光辉，一片玫瑰色、金色，闪着薄雾的微光。

===Original===
“This! The﻿—the cabbages.”

===Chinese===
「这个！这——卷心菜。」

===Original===
A slow-dawning film of fun crept over the blue of Klaas Pool’s stare. This film spread almost imperceptibly so that it fluted his broad nostrils, met and widened his full lips, reached and agitated his massive shoulders, tickled the round belly, so that all Klaas Pool, from his eyes to his waist, was rippling and shaking with slow, solemn, heavy Dutch mirth.

===Chinese===
一层迟迟才泛起的笑意，悄悄爬上克拉斯·普尔凝望的蓝色。这层笑意几乎觉察不出地漫开来：撑圆了他宽大的鼻孔，漫到他厚实的嘴唇上把嘴撑宽，够着他那厚墩墩的肩膀、掀得直颤，又去挠他圆滚滚的肚皮，于是整个克拉斯·普尔，从眼睛到腰，全都涟漪似的抖起来、颤起来，抖着一种缓慢、庄重、沉甸甸的荷兰式的乐子。

===Original===
“Cabbages is beautiful!” his round pop eyes staring at her in a fixity of glee. “Cabbages is beautiful!” His silent laughter now rose and became audible in a rich throaty chortle. It was plain that laughter, with Klaas Pool, was not a thing to be lightly dismissed, once raised. “Cabbages﻿—” he choked a little, and spluttered, overcome. Now he began to shift his gaze back to his horses and the road, by the same process of turning his head first and then his eyes, so that to Selina the mirthful tail of his right eye and his round red cheek with the golden fuzz on it gave him an incredibly roguish brownie look.

===Chinese===
「卷心菜是美的！」他那双圆鼓鼓的眼睛喜滋滋地定定望着她。「卷心菜是美的！」他那无声的笑这时涨了上来，出了声，成了喉咙里滚出的一串浑厚的呵呵。很明显，笑这件事，在克拉斯·普尔身上，一经唤起，可不是能轻轻打发掉的。「卷心菜——」他噎了一下，喷着沫子，笑得招架不住。随后他开始把目光挪回他的马和路上去，还是那套程序：先转头，再转眼睛——于是在塞莉娜看来，他右眼梢那条笑出来的尾巴，连同他覆着金色绒毛的圆红脸膛，给了他一副说不出的、小棕仙（brownie）式的淘气神气。

===Original===
Selina laughed, too, even while she protested his laughter. “But they are!” she insisted. “They *are* beautiful. Like jade and Burgundy. No, like﻿—uh﻿—like﻿—what’s that in﻿—like chrysoprase and porphyry. All those fields of cabbages and the corn and the beet-tops together look like Persian patches.”

===Chinese===
塞莉娜也笑了，一边笑一边还抗议他的笑。「可它们就是美的呀！」她坚持道。「它们*就是*美。像玉，像勃艮第。不，像——呃——像——那叫什么来着——像绿玉髓，像斑岩。那一片片的卷心菜田，还有玉米、甜菜叶，连在一处，看去像一块块波斯花毯。」

===Original===
Which was, certainly, no way for a new school teacher to talk to a Holland truck gardener driving his team along the dirt road on his way to High Prairie. But then, Selina, remember, had read Byron at seventeen.

===Chinese===
这话，实在不是一个新上任的女教师该对一位赶着车、走土路回高草原去的荷兰菜农说的话。可是别忘了，塞莉娜十七岁上就已经读过拜伦了。

===Original===
Klaas Pool knew nothing of chrysoprase and porphyry. Nor of Byron. Nor, for that matter, of jade and Burgundy. But he did know cabbages, both green and red. He knew cabbage from seed to sauerkraut; he knew and grew varieties from the sturdy Flat Dutch to the early Wakefield. But that they were beautiful; that they looked like jewels; that they lay like Persian patches, had never entered his head, and rightly. What has the head of a cabbage, or, for that matter, of a robust, soil-stained, toiling Dutch truck farmer to do with nonsense like chrysoprase, with jade, with Burgundy, with Persian patterns!

===Chinese===
克拉斯·普尔对绿玉髓、对斑岩一无所知。对拜伦也一无所知。说起来，对玉、对勃艮第一样一无所知。可卷心菜他是懂的，绿的红的都懂。从一粒种子到一缸酸菜，卷心菜的底细他没有不知道的；从皮实的平地荷兰种（Flat Dutch）到早熟的韦克菲尔德（Wakefield），各色品种他都认得、都种过。可是说它们美；说它们像宝石；说它们像波斯花毯似的铺展着——这样的念头从没进过他的脑子，而且理当如此。一颗卷心菜的脑瓜——或者说，一个壮实、沾着泥土、终日劳作的荷兰菜农的脑瓜——跟绿玉髓这路胡话有什么相干，跟玉、跟勃艮第、跟波斯花样有什么相干！

===Original===
The horses clopped down the heavy country road. Now and again the bulk beside Selina was agitated silently, as before. And from between the golden fuzz of stubble beard she would hear, “Cabbages! Cabbages is﻿—” But she did not feel offended. She could not have been offended at anything today. For in spite of her recent tragedy, her nineteen years, her loneliness, the terrifying thought of this new home to which she was going, among strangers, she was conscious of a warm little thrill of elation, of excitement﻿—of adventure! That was it. “The whole thing’s just a grand adventure,” Simeon Peake had said. Selina gave a little bounce of anticipation. She was doing a revolutionary and daring thing; a thing that the Vermont and now, fortunately, inaccessible Peakes would have regarded with horror. For equipment she had youth, curiosity, a steel-strong frame; one brown lady’s-cloth, one wine-red cashmere; four hundred and ninety-seven dollars; and a gay, adventuresome spirit that was never to die, though it led her into curious places and she often found, at the end, only a trackless waste from which she had to retrace her steps, painfully. But always, to her, red and green cabbages were to be jade and Burgundy, chrysoprase and porphyry. Life has no weapons against a woman like that.

===Chinese===
马蹄嘚嘚地敲在沉甸甸的乡路上。塞莉娜身旁那座肉山时不时又无声地抖动起来，像先前一样。于是她会听见金色胡茬之间飘出一句：「卷心菜！卷心菜是——」可她并不觉得受了冒犯。这一天，无论什么都冒犯不了她。因为尽管新遭惨变，尽管她只有十九岁、形单影只，一想到要去的那个新家、那一群陌生人便心里发慌，她却觉出一股暖乎乎的小小颤栗，一阵兴头——是冒险！对了，就是这个。「整个这件事，不过是一场堂堂的大冒险。」西缅·皮克说过。塞莉娜满怀期待，身子轻轻一颠。她正在做一件离经叛道、胆大包天的事；一件会让佛蒙特的那班皮克家——幸而如今再够也够不着他们了——吓得魂不附体的事。论行装，她有青春，有好奇心，有一副钢筋铁骨的身子架；一身棕色女装呢，一身酒红开司米；四百九十七美元；还有一腔快活的、爱冒险的精气神——这腔精气神至死不灭，尽管它领着她闯进过种种稀奇古怪的地方，而她常常到了头才发现，那里只有一片不见路踪的荒野，她只得忍着痛，一步一步循原路折返。可是无论到几时，红的绿的卷心菜，在她眼里永远是玉和勃艮第，是绿玉髓和斑岩。生活对这样的女人，是拿不出武器来的。

===Original===
So now, as they bumped and jolted along the road Selina thought herself lucky, though she was a little terrified. She turned her gaze from the flat prairie land to the silent figure beside her. Hers was a lively, volatile nature, and his uncommunicativeness made her vaguely uncomfortable. Yet there was nothing glum about his face. Upon it there even lingered, in the corners of his eyes and about his mouth, faint shadows of merriment.

===Chinese===
于是此刻，车子一路磕磕绊绊、颠颠簸簸，塞莉娜觉得自己运气好，虽然心里也有些发怵。她把目光从平坦的草原上收回来，落到身旁那个沉默的人影上。她的性子活泼好动，他这闷不吭声叫她隐隐地不大自在。可他脸上又没有半点阴沉。眼角边、嘴角边，甚至还真留着淡淡的笑影。

===Original===
Klaas Pool was a school director. She was to live at his house. Perhaps she should not have said that about the cabbages. So now she drew herself up primly and tried to appear the school teacher, and succeeded in looking as severe as a white pansy.

===Chinese===
克拉斯·普尔是学董。她就要住在他家里。方才关于卷心菜的那番话，她也许不该说的。于是这会儿她端端正正坐直了身子，努力摆出女教师的做派来，结果只落得一本正经得像朵白三色堇。

===Original===
“Ahem!” (or nearly that). “You have three children, haven’t you, Mr. Pool? They’ll all be my pupils?”

===Chinese===
「啊哼！」（或是差不多的一个音）。「您有三个孩子，是吧，普尔先生？他们全都要做我的学生吧？」

===Original===
Klaas Pool ruminated on this. He concentrated so that a slight frown marred the serenity of his brow. In this double question of hers, an attempt to give the conversation a dignified turn, she had apparently created some difficulty for her host. He was trying to shake his head two ways at the same time. This gave it a rotary motion. Selina saw, with amazement, that he was attempting to nod negation and confirmation at once.

===Chinese===
克拉斯·普尔对这个问题反刍起来。他那样专注，眉宇的安详竟让一道轻轻的皱褶破了相。她这一语双问——本想叫谈话端庄严整地转个弯——显然给她这位东道出了道难题。他正试着同时朝两边摇头。这一来，脑袋便转起了圈。塞莉娜惊愕地看到，他是在努力又点头又摇头，想把否定和肯定一气点齐。

===Original===
“You mean you haven’t﻿—or they’re not?﻿—or﻿—?”

===Chinese===
「您是说您没有——还是他们不——还是——？」

===Original===
“I have got three children. All will not be your pupils.” There was something final, unshakable in his delivery of this.

===Chinese===
「我有三个孩子。不全都会做你的学生。」他这话吐出来，斩钉截铁，撼不动摇不得。

===Original===
“Dear me! Why not? Which ones won’t?”

===Chinese===
「哎呀！为什么不全？哪几个不呢？」

===Original===
This fusillade proved fatal. It served permanently to check the slight trickle of conversation which had begun to issue from his lips. They jogged on for perhaps a matter of three miles, in silence. Selina told herself then, sternly, that she must not laugh. Having told herself this, sternly, she began to laugh because she could not help it; a gay little sound that flew out like the whir of a bird’s wing on the crisp autumnal sunset air. And suddenly this light sound was joined by a slow rumbling that swelled and bubbled a good deal in the manner of the rich glubby sounds that issue from a kettle that has been simmering for a long time. So they laughed together, these two; the rather scared young thing who was trying to be prim, and the dull, unimaginative truck farmer because this alert, great-eyed, slim white creature perched birdlike on the wagon seat beside him had tickled his slow humour-sense.

===Chinese===
这一梭子连珠炮，后果是致命的。它把方才刚开始从他唇间渗出的那一丝谈话的细流，永久地堵断了。两人又闷头走了约莫三英里，一路无言。塞莉娜于是狠狠告诫自己，千万不可笑。如此狠狠告诫过自己之后，她便笑了起来，因为忍不住；一小串轻快的笑声，像鸟翅的扑棱，飞进清冽的秋暮空气里。突然，这轻快的声音里加入了一阵缓慢的隆隆，那声音涨着、咕嘟着，活像一只煨了许久的壶里滚出的那种厚嘟嘟、油润润的声响。于是这两人便一同笑了：一个是心头发怯、努力端着的小东西，一个是迟钝、没想象的菜农——因为这只机灵的、大眼睛的、纤细白净的造物，鸟儿似的栖在他身旁的车座上，搔着了他那根慢吞吞的幽默神经。

===Original===
Selina felt suddenly friendly and happy. “Do tell me which ones will and which won’t.”

===Chinese===
塞莉娜忽然觉得又亲热又快活。「快告诉我吧，哪几个会上，哪几个不会。」

===Original===
“Geertje goes to school. Jozina goes to school. Roelf works by the farm.”

===Chinese===
「海尔切（Geertje）上学。约西娜（Jozina）上学。鲁尔夫（Roelf）在农场做活。」

===Original===
“How old is Roelf?” She was being school teacherly again.

===Chinese===
「鲁尔夫多大了？」她又端起女教师的架子来。

===Original===
“Roelf is twelve.”

===Chinese===
「鲁尔夫十二了。」

===Original===
“Twelve! And no longer at school! But why not!”

===Chinese===
「十二岁！就不上学了！可这是为什么！」

===Original===
“Roelf he works by the farm.”

===Chinese===
「鲁尔夫他是在农场做活。」

===Original===
“Doesn’t Roelf like school?”

===Chinese===
「鲁尔夫不喜欢上学吗？」

===Original===
“But sure.”

===Chinese===
「那当然。」

===Original===
“Don’t you think he ought to go to school?”

===Chinese===
「您不觉得他应该上学去吗？」

===Original===
“But sure.”

===Chinese===
「那当然。」

===Original===
Having begun, she could not go back. “Doesn’t your wife want Roelf to go to school any more?”

===Chinese===
话既开了头，便收不回了。「您太太不想再让鲁尔夫上学了吗？」

===Original===
“Maartje? But sure.”

===Chinese===
「玛尔切（Maartje）？那当然。」

===Original===
She gathered herself together; hurled herself behind the next question. “Then why *doesn’t* he go to school, for pity’s sake!”

===Chinese===
她攒足了全身的劲，把下一问整个掷了出去。「那他*到底为什么不*去上学呢，我的老天！」

===Original===
Klaas Pool’s pale blue eyes were fixed on the spot between the horse’s ears. His face was serene, placid, patient.

===Chinese===
克拉斯·普尔那双淡蓝的眼睛，定在马耳朵之间的那个点上。他一脸的安详、平和、耐心。

===Original===
“Roelf he works by the farm.”

===Chinese===
「鲁尔夫他是在农场做活。」

===Original===
Selina subsided, beaten.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜偃旗息鼓，败下阵来。

===Original===
She wondered about Roelf. Would he be a furtive, slinking boy, like Smike? Geertje and Jozina. Geertje﻿—Gertrude, of course. Jozina? Josephine. Maartje?﻿—m-m-m-m﻿—Martha, probably. At any rate, it was going to be interesting. It was going to be wonderful! Suppose she had gone to Vermont and become a dried apple!

===Chinese===
她心里琢磨起鲁尔夫来。他会是个畏畏缩缩、溜边儿走的男孩吗，像斯迈克（Smike）那样？海尔切和约西娜。海尔切——自然是格特鲁德（Gertrude）。约西娜呢？约瑟芬（Josephine）。玛尔切？——嗯-嗯-嗯-嗯——多半是玛莎（Martha）。无论如何，往后的日子会是有趣的。会是妙极了的！试想她当初若是回了佛蒙特，做了一只蔫干瘪的苹果！

===Original===
Dusk was coming on. The lake mist came drifting across the prairie and hung, a pearly haze, over the frost-nipped stubble and the leafless trees. It caught the last light in the sky, and held it, giving to fields, trees, black earth, to the man seated stolidly beside the girl, and to the face of the girl herself an opalescent glow very wonderful to see. Selina, seeing it, opened her lips to exclaim again; and then, remembering, closed them. She had learned her first lesson in High Prairie.

===Chinese===
暮色四合。湖上的雾飘过草原，悬在那里，成了一层珠色的薄翳，罩住霜咬过的残茬和落尽了叶的树。它接住天上最后一点光，又把它留住，给田野、树木、黑土，给木然坐在姑娘身旁的男人，也给姑娘自己的那张脸，都敷上一层乳光流转的辉彩，好看极了。塞莉娜看在眼里，张口又要惊叹；随即想起什么，又闭上了。她在高草原上了头一课。

## III / 第三章

===Original===
The Klaas Pools lived in a typical High Prairie house. They had passed a score like it in the dusk. These sturdy Holland-Americans had built here in Illinois after the pattern of the squat houses that dot the lowlands about Amsterdam, Haarlem, and Rotterdam. A row of pollards stood stiffly by the roadside. As they turned in at the yard Selina’s eye was caught by the glitter of glass. The house was many-windowed, the panes the size of pocket-handkerchiefs. Even in the dusk Selina thought she had never seen windows sparkle so. She did not then know that spotless windowpanes were a mark of social standing in High Prairie. Yard and dwelling had a geometrical neatness like that of a toy house in a set of playthings. The effect was marred by a clothesline hung with a dado of miscellaneous wash﻿—a pair of faded overalls, a shirt, socks, a man’s drawers carefully patched and now bellying grotesquely in the breeze like a comic tramp turned bacchanal. Selina was to know this frieze of nether garments as a daily decoration in the farm-wife’s yard.

===Chinese===
克拉斯·普尔家住的是一座典型的高草原房子。暮色里，他们一路已经走过二十来座一模一样的。这些结结实实的荷兰裔美国人，照着阿姆斯特丹、哈勒姆（Haarlem）、鹿特丹（Rotterdam）四周低地上星星点点的那些低矮房子的式样，在伊利诺伊这儿盖起了自己的家。路边一排截头树直挺挺地立着。车子拐进院子时，一片玻璃的闪光攫住了塞莉娜的眼睛。这房子开着一扇扇窗，玻璃只有手帕大小。即便在暮色里，塞莉娜也觉得从没见过哪家的窗玻璃这样亮。她那时还不知道，一尘不染的窗玻璃，在高草原上是身分体面的标志。院子和住房有种几何图形般的齐整，像一套玩具里的小房子。只是这效果叫一根晾衣绳破了相：绳上垂着一圈五花八门的换洗衣裳，像给房子添了道墙裙——一条褪色的工装裤，一件衬衫，几双袜子，还有一条细心补缀过的男人衬裤，这会儿正在风里古里古怪地鼓着胀着，活像个改行当酒神的滑稽流浪汉。塞莉娜日后会知道，这条下身衣物缀成的横饰带，是农妇院子里天天张挂的装饰。

===Original===
Peering down over the high wheel she waited for Klaas Pool to assist her in alighting. He seemed to have no such thought. Having jumped down, he was throwing empty crates and boxes out of the back of the wagon. So Selina, gathering her shawls and cloak about her, clambered down the side of the wheel and stood looking about her in the dim light, a very small figure in a very large world. Klaas had opened the barn door. Now he returned and slapped one of the horses smartly on the flank. The team trotted obediently off to the barn. He picked up her little hidebound trunk. She took her satchel. The yard was quite dark now. As Klaas Pool opened the kitchen door the red mouth that was the open draught in the kitchen stove grinned a toothy welcome at them.

===Chinese===
她从高高的车轮上探身下望，等着克拉斯·普尔来扶她下车。他似乎压根儿没有这个念头。他自己跳下车后，正把空筐空箱一个个从车后扔下来。于是塞莉娜拢起披肩和斗篷，顺着车轮一侧爬下来，站在昏暗里四下打量——一个极小的人影，立在一个极大的世界里。克拉斯已经开了谷仓的门。这时他走回来，在一匹马的腰窝上利落地拍了一巴掌，两匹马便乖乖地小跑着进谷仓去了。他拎起她那口包革的小箱子。她提起自己的挎包。院子已经全黑了。克拉斯·普尔推开厨房门时，厨房炉灶那敞开的通火口像一张红嘴，龇着一口牙齿，笑吟吟地迎他们进来。

===Original===
A woman stood over the stove, a fork in her hand. The kitchen was clean, but disorderly, with the disorder that comes of pressure of work. There was a not unpleasant smell of cooking. Selina sniffed it hungrily. The woman turned to face them. Selina stared.

===Chinese===
一个女人站在炉灶前，手里握着一把叉子。厨房干净，却凌乱，是被活计压出来的那种凌乱。屋里有一股烧饭的气味，倒不难闻。塞莉娜饥肠辘辘地吸了吸鼻子。那女人转过身来面对他们。塞莉娜瞪住了。

===Original===
This, she thought, must be some other﻿—an old woman﻿—his mother perhaps. But: “Maartje, here is school teacher,” said Klaas Pool. Selina put out her hand to meet the other woman’s hand, rough, hard, calloused. Her own, touching it, was like satin against a pine board. Maartje smiled, and you saw her broken discoloured teeth. She pushed back the sparse hair from her high forehead, fumbled a little, shyly, at the collar of her clean blue calico dress.

===Chinese===
这一个，她想，一定另有其人——是个老妇人——兴许是他母亲。可是：「玛尔切，女教师来了。」克拉斯·普尔说。塞莉娜伸出手去，迎上那只伸过来的女人的手——粗糙、僵硬、满是老茧。她自己的手碰上去，就像缎子贴着松木板。玛尔切笑了，露出一口磕坏发暗的牙。她把稀疏的头发从高高的额头上撩回去，又怯生生地摸了摸她那件干净的蓝印花布裙的领口。

===Original===
“Pleased to meet you,” Maartje said, primly. “Make you welcome.” Then, as Pool stamped out to the yard, slamming the door behind him, “Pool he could have come with you by the front way, too. Lay off your things.” Selina began to remove the wrappings that swathed her﻿—the muffler, the shawl, the cloak. Now she stood, a slim, incongruously elegant little figure in that kitchen. The brown lady’s-cloth was very tight and basqued above, very flounced and bustled below. “My, how you are young!” cried Maartje. She moved nearer, as if impelled, and fingered the stuff of Selina’s gown. And as she did this Selina suddenly saw that she, too, was young. The bad teeth, the thin hair, the careless dress, the littered kitchen, the harassed frown﻿—above all these, standing out clearly, appeared the look of a girl.

===Chinese===
「高兴见着你。」玛尔切一板一眼地说。「欢迎欢迎。」随后，等普尔咚咚地踩着步子出去进院子、把门摔上，她又补了一句：「普尔他本来也可以陪你从前门进的嘛。把你的东西卸了吧。」塞莉娜开始解开裹在身上的层层围裹——围脖、披肩、斗篷。这会儿她站在那里，在那间厨房里成了一个纤瘦的、雅致得格格不入的小小身影。那身棕色女装呢，上身收得又紧又合体，下身却荷叶边层层、裙垫鼓鼓。「哎呀，你可真年轻！」玛尔切叫道。她像被什么驱使着，凑近来摸了摸塞莉娜裙子的料子。就在她摸着的时候，塞莉娜突然看见：她自己，也年轻。那口坏牙、那头稀发、那身随随便便的衣裳、那乱糟糟的厨房、那副焦灼的愁眉——越过这一切，清清楚楚地浮出了一张少女的脸。

===Original===
“Why, I do believe she’s not more than twenty-eight!” Selina said to herself in a kind of panic. “I do believe she’s not more than twenty-eight.”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，我看她准不到二十八！」塞莉娜有点慌神地对自己说。「我看她准不到二十八。」

===Original===
She had been aware of the two pigtailed heads appearing and vanishing in the doorway of the next room. Now Maartje was shooing her into this room. Evidently her hostess was distressed because the school teacher’s formal entrance had not been made by way of parlour instead of kitchen. She followed Maartje Pool into the front room. Behind the stove, tittering, were two yellow-haired little girls. Geertje and Jozina, of course. Selina went over to them, smiling. “Which is Geertje?” she asked. “And which Jozina?” But at this the titters became squeals. They retired behind the round black bulwark of the woodburner, overcome. There was no fire in this shining ebon structure, though the evening was sharp. Above the stove a length of pipe, glittering with polish as was the stove itself, crossed the width of the room and vanished through a queer little perforated grating in the ceiling. Selina’s quick glance encompassed the room. In the window were a few hardy plants in pots on a green-painted wooden rack. There were geraniums, blossomless; a cactus with its thick slabs of petals like slices of gangrenous ham set up for beauty in a parlour; a plant called Jacob’s ladder, on a spindling trellis. The bony scaffolding of the green-painted wooden stand was turned toward the room. The flowers blindly faced the dark square of the window. There was a sofa with a wrinkled calico cover; three rocking chairs; some stark crayons of incredibly hard-featured Dutch ancients on the wall. It was all neat, stiff, unlovely. But Selina had known too many years of boardinghouse ugliness to be offended at this.

===Chinese===
她早就觉察到隔壁房间门口有两颗扎辫子的小脑袋忽而一探、忽而一缩。这会儿玛尔切正把她往那间屋里让。显然，女教师正经进门的礼数没能走客厅而是走了厨房，这让女主人很是不安。她跟着玛尔切·普尔进了前屋。炉子背后，两个黄头发小姑娘正咯咯地笑。自然是海尔切和约西娜。塞莉娜含笑朝她们走过去。「哪个是海尔切？」她问。「哪个是约西娜？」谁知这一问，咯咯声变成了尖叫。两个小姑娘招架不住，缩回那柴炉圆圆的黑色壁垒后面去了。这座黑得发亮的乌木家伙里并没有生火，虽然晚来天寒。炉子上方一截烟囱管，通体擦得和炉子本身一样锃亮，横贯屋子，钻进天花板上一个古怪的小孔格栅，不见了。塞莉娜目光一扫，把屋子收在眼里。窗台上是一只绿漆木架，架上摆着几盆皮实的花草：几棵天竺葵，没开花；一棵仙人掌，肥厚的瓣像一片片坏疽的火腿，立在客厅里供人赏玩；还有一盆名叫「雅各的天梯」（Jacob’s ladder）的植物，攀着一架细脚伶仃的格子。那绿漆木架嶙峋的骨架朝着屋里；花草们则懵然朝着窗上那一方黑。屋里有一张沙发，铺着皱巴巴的印花布罩；三把摇椅；墙上挂着几幅笔画生硬的荷兰老祖宗的蜡笔画，一张张面目硬朗得叫人不敢信。这一切都干净、僵直、不讨人喜欢。可是塞莉娜在包伙公寓的丑陋里泡的年头太多了，早不至于为此见怪。

===Original===
Maartje had lighted a small glass-bowled lamp. The chimney of this sparkled as had the window panes. A steep, uncarpeted stairway, enclosed, led off the sitting room. Up this Maartje Pool, talking, led the way to Selina’s bedroom. Selina was to learn that the farm woman, often inarticulate through lack of companionship, becomes a torrent of talk when opportunity presents itself. They made quite a little procession. First, Mrs. Pool with the lamp; then Selina with the satchel; then, tap-tap, tap-tap, Jozina and Geertje, their heavy hobnailed shoes creating a great clatter on the wooden stairs, though they were tiptoeing in an effort to make themselves unheard by their mother. There evidently had been an arrangement on the subject of their invisibility. The procession moved to the accompaniment of Maartje’s, “Now you stay downstairs didn’t I tell you!” There was in her tone a warning; a menace. The two pigtails would hang back a moment, only to come tap-tapping on again, their saucer eyes at once fearful and mischievous.

===Chinese===
玛尔切点起了一盏玻璃油灯。灯罩也和窗玻璃一样闪闪发亮。一道陡陡的封闭楼梯，不铺地毯，从起居室边通上楼去。玛尔切·普尔打着话头，一路领着塞莉娜上她的卧室去。塞莉娜日后会懂得：农妇平日没人做伴，嘴都使木了，一旦有了机会，便成了话的洪流。他们这一行好生成了一支小小的队伍：打头是端灯的普尔太太；随后是挎包的塞莉娜；再后头，嗒-嗒，嗒-嗒，是约西娜和海尔切——两双钉铁掌的笨重鞋子在木头楼梯上敲出好大一片响，其实她们正踮着脚尖，一心想叫母亲听不见。关于她们两个应当隐形一节，母女间显然早有成约。这支队伍一路行进，伴奏是玛尔切的「给我待在楼下，没听见吗！」——口气里有警告，有威吓。两条辫子会迟疑片刻，随即又嗒-嗒地跟上来，一双双茶碟似的眼睛又怕又坏。

===Original===
A narrow, dim, close-smelling hallway, uncarpeted. At the end of it a door opening into the room that was to be Selina’s. As its chill struck her to the marrow three objects caught her eye. The bed, a huge and not unhandsome walnut mausoleum, reared its sombre height almost to the room’s top. Indeed, its apex of grapes did actually seem to achieve a meeting with the whitewashed ceiling. The mattress of straw and cornhusks was unworthy of this edifice, but over it Mrs. Pool had mercifully placed a feather bed, stitched and quilted, so that Selina lay soft and warm through the winter. Along one wall stood a low chest so richly brown as to appear black. The front panel of this was curiously carved. Selina stooped before it and for the second time that day said: “How beautiful!” then looked quickly round at Maartje Pool as though fearful of finding her laughing as Klaas Pool had laughed. But Mrs. Pool’s face reflected the glow in her own. She came over to Selina and stooped with her over the chest, holding the lamp so that its yellow flame lighted up the scrolls and tendrils of the carved surface. With one discoloured forefinger she traced the bold flourishes on the panel. “See? How it makes out letters?”

===Chinese===
一条窄窄的、昏暗的、有股闷味的过道，不铺地毯。尽头一扇门，通向就要归塞莉娜的那间屋子。寒气刺骨时，三样东西攫住了她的目光。那张床，一座庞大而不失体面的胡桃木陵墓，阴沉沉地高高耸起，几乎顶到屋顶。真的，床顶端那一嘟噜葡萄，看上去当真同刷白的天花板碰上了。稻草和玉米皮絮的床垫配不上这座殿堂，好在普尔太太发慈心，在上头铺了一床绗缝的羽毛褥子，好叫塞莉娜一冬睡得软和暖和。沿一面墙立着一口矮柜，棕色深得近乎发黑。柜子的正面镶板雕着古雅的花样。塞莉娜俯下身去，这一天里第二次说道：「多美啊！」随即飞快地回头看玛尔切·普尔，生怕发现她也像克拉斯·普尔那样笑起来。可是普尔太太脸上正映着她自己眼里那点亮光。她走到塞莉娜身边，陪她俯在柜前，举着灯，让黄澄澄的火苗照亮雕面上流转的卷纹和藤蔓。她用一根变了色的食指描着面板上遒劲的花体字。「看见没？它拼出字母来呢？」

===Original===
Selina peered closer. “Why, sure enough! This first one’s an S!”

===Chinese===
塞莉娜凑近细看。「哎呀，可不是！头一个是个 S！」

===Original===
Maartje was kneeling before the chest now. “Sure an S. For Sophia. It is a Holland bride’s chest. And here is K. And here is big D. It makes Sophia Kroon DeVries. It is anyways two hundred years. My mother she gave it to me when I was married, and her mother she gave it to her when she was married, and her mother gave it to her when she was married, and her﻿—”

===Chinese===
玛尔切这会儿已跪在柜前。「可不是个 S。索菲娅（Sophia）的 S。这是口荷兰新娘箱。这儿是个 K。这儿是个大 D。拼出来是索菲娅·克龙·德弗里斯（Sophia Kroon DeVries）。少说也有二百年了。我出嫁的时候，我妈把它给了我；我妈出嫁的时候，她的妈给了她；她出嫁的时候，她的妈又给了她；还有她的——」

===Original===
“I should think so!” exclaimed Selina, rather meaninglessly; but stemming the torrent. “What’s in it? Anything? There ought to be bride’s clothes in it, yellow with age.”

===Chinese===
「我想也是！」塞莉娜喊道，实在没什么意义，只为拦住这股洪流。「里头有什么没有？总该有件把嫁衣，放得焦黄了吧。」

===Original===
“It is!” cried Maartje Pool and gave a little bounce that imperilled the lamp.

===Chinese===
「有的呀！」玛尔切·普尔叫道，身子轻轻一颠，险些把灯颠翻了。

===Original===
“No!” The two on their knees sat smiling at each other, wide-eyed, like schoolgirls. The pigtails, emboldened, had come tap-tapping nearer and were peering over the shoulders of the women before the chest.

===Chinese===
「不会吧！」跪着的两个女人坐在脚后跟上，睁圆了眼睛相视而笑，像一对女学生。两条辫子胆子壮了，嗒-嗒地凑近前来，从两个女人肩后探看那口柜子。

===Original===
“Here﻿—wait.” Maartje Pool thrust the lamp into Selina’s hand, raised the lid of the chest, dived expertly into its depths amidst a great rustling of old newspapers and emerged red-faced with a Dutch basque and voluminous skirt of silk; an age-yellow cap whose wings, stiff with embroidery, stood out grandly on either side; a pair of wooden shoes, stained terra-cotta like the sails of the Vollendam fishing boats, and carved from toe to heel in a delicate and intricate pattern. A bridal gown, a bridal cap, bridal shoes.

===Chinese===
「这儿——等等。」玛尔切·普尔把灯往塞莉娜手里一塞，掀开箱盖，在一阵旧报纸的哗啦声里熟练地探进箱底，红着脸捧出一身行头：一件荷兰式紧身短上衣，一条肥大的绸裙；一顶黄了年头的老式帽，帽翅让绣活浆得挺硬，气派地支在两旁；还有一双木鞋，染着陶瓦红，像沃伦丹（Vollendam）渔船的帆，从鞋尖到鞋跟镂满细巧繁复的花纹。一件新娘礼服，一顶新娘帽，一双新娘鞋。

===Original===
“Well!” said Selina, with the feeling of a little girl in a rich attic on a rainy day. She clasped her hands. “May I dress up in it some time?”

===Chinese===
「哎呀！」塞莉娜说，那心情活像个雨天蹲在阔气阁楼里的小姑娘。她把双手扣在一起。「哪天我能穿上它过过瘾吗？」

===Original===
Maartje Pool, folding the garments hastily, looked shocked and horrified. “Never must anybody dress up in a bride’s dress only to get married. It brings bad luck.” Then, as Selina stroked the stiff silken folds of the skirt with a slim and caressing forefinger: “So you get married to a High Prairie Dutchman I let you wear it.” At this absurdity they both laughed again. Selina thought that this school-teaching venture was starting out very well. She would have *such* things to tell her father﻿—then she remembered. She shivered a little as she stood up now. She raised her arms to take off her hat, feeling suddenly tired, cold, strange in this house with this farm woman, and the two staring little girls, and the great red-faced man. There surged over her a great wave of longing for her father﻿—for the gay little dinners, for the theatre treats, for his humorous philosophical drawl, for the Chicago streets, and the ugly Chicago houses; for Julie; for Miss Fister’s school; for anything and anyone that was accustomed, known, and therefore dear. Even Aunt Abbie and Aunt Sarah had a not unlovely aspect, viewed from this chill farmhouse bedroom that had suddenly become her home. She had a horrible premonition that she was going to cry, began to blink very fast, turned a little blindly in the dim light and caught sight of the room’s third arresting object. A blue-black cylinder of tin sheeting, like a stove and yet unlike. It was polished like the length of pipe in the sitting room below. Indeed, it was evidently a giant flower of this stem.

===Chinese===
玛尔切·普尔慌忙叠起衣裳，一脸的骇然。「新娘的礼服，除了结婚，谁也万万穿不得。要遭晦气的。」接着，见塞莉娜用一根纤细的爱抚的食指抚过裙上挺硬的绸褶，又道：「等你嫁了高草原的荷兰人，我就让你穿。」这句荒唐话又引得两人大笑。塞莉娜觉得，这趟教书的事，开头开得实在不错。她会有*那么多*新鲜事讲给父亲听——随即她想起了。这会儿她站起身，微微打了个寒噤。她抬起双臂去摘帽子，忽然觉得疲倦、寒冷，在这座房子里、在这农妇、两个瞪眼瞧人的小丫头和那个大红脸汉子中间，满心的生疏。一阵大浪劈头盖脸地涌上来，是对父亲的思念——思念那些快活的小小晚餐、看戏的乐子、他那幽默而带哲学味儿的慢腔慢调；思念芝加哥的街道、芝加哥那些丑房子；思念朱莉；思念菲斯特小姐的学校；思念一切习惯的、熟识的、因而可爱的人和物。连艾比姑姑和萨拉姑姑，从这间骤然成了她的家的、寒气袭人的农舍卧室里望回去，眉眼也显得不无情分了。她有一种可怕的预感，觉得自己就要哭出来，眼睛开始飞快地眨，在昏暗里微微失神地一转身，正瞧见这屋里第三件攫人的东西。一个蓝黑色的铁皮圆筒，像炉子，又不是炉子。它擦得同楼下起居室里那截烟囱管一样亮。说真的，它分明就是那根管茎顶上开出的一朵大得吓人的花。

===Original===
“What’s that?” demanded Selina, pointing.

===Chinese===
「那是什么？」塞莉娜指着问。

===Original===
Maartje Pool, depositing the lamp on the little washstand preparatory to leaving, smiled pridefully. “Drum.”

===Chinese===
玛尔切·普尔预备走了，把灯搁在小脸盆架上，得意地一笑。「鼓。」

===Original===
“Drum?”

===Chinese===
「鼓？」

===Original===
“For heat your room.” Selina touched it. It was icy. “When there is fire,” Mrs. Pool added, hastily. In her mind’s eye Selina traced the tin tube below running along the ceiling in the peaceful and orderly path of a stovepipe, thrusting its way through the cylindrical hole in the ceiling and here bursting suddenly into swollen and monstrous bloom like an unthinkable goitre on a black neck. Selina was to learn that its heating powers were mythical. Even when the stove in the sitting room was blazing away with a cheerful roar none of the glow communicated itself to the drum. It remained as coolly indifferent to the blasts breathed upon it as a girl hotly besieged by an unwelcome lover. This was to influence a number of Selina’s habits, including nocturnal reading and matutinal bathing. Selina was a daily morning bather in a period which looked upon the daily bath as an eccentricity, or, at best, an affectation. It would be charming to be able to record that she continued the practice in the Pool household; but a morning bath in the arctic atmosphere of an Illinois prairie farmhouse would not have been eccentric merely, but mad, even if there had been an available kettle of hot water at 6:30 a.m., which there emphatically was not. Selina was grateful for an occasional steaming basin of water at night and a hurried piecemeal bath by the mythical heat of the drum.

===Chinese===
「好暖你的屋。」塞莉娜摸了摸它。冰凉。「等生了火就暖了。」普尔太太赶忙补上一句。塞莉娜在心里描画楼下那根铁皮管子：它沿着天花板，规规矩矩地走一条烟囱管的路，从天花板那个圆洞里挤上来，到了这里，忽然膨开一朵臃肿怪异的花，像黑脖子上一个想都不敢想的甲状腺瘤。塞莉娜日后会懂得，它的暖人之力纯属神话。哪怕楼下起居室的炉子烧得欢畅轰响，那点热乎气儿也一丝儿传不到这面鼓上来。任凭人朝它呵多少热气，它始终冷冷地不为所动，活像个被不情愿的求亲者围追堵截的姑娘。这一点日后要左右塞莉娜的一串习惯，包括夜里念书，也包括清早洗浴。塞莉娜是天天清早都要洗澡的人，而在那个年头，天天洗澡在世人眼里不是怪癖，至多也是矫情。倘若能记上一笔，说她在普尔家仍守着这个习惯，那自然是桩美谈；可是在伊利诺伊草原农舍那北极一般的空气里，清早洗澡就不止是怪癖，简直是发疯——就算清早 6:30 真能有一壶现成的热水也罢，何况铁定没有。塞莉娜于是心怀感激地领受夜里偶尔的一盆滚烫的水，就着那面鼓神话般的热气，匆匆地零打碎敲洗上一气。

===Original===
“Maartje!” roared a voice from belowstairs. The voice of the hungry male. There was wafted up, too, a faint smell of scorching. Then came sounds of a bumping and thumping along the narrow stairway.

===Chinese===
「玛尔切！」楼下轰起一声吼。饿汉子的嗓门。跟着，一缕烤糊了的焦味也飘了上来。接着是狭窄楼梯上一路咚咚咣咣、磕磕绊绊的声响。

===Original===
“*Og heden!*” cried Maartje, in a panic, her hands high in air. She was off, sweeping the two pigtails with her in her flight. There were sounds of scuffling on the stairway, and Maartje’s voice calling something that sounded like hookendunk to Selina. But she decided that that couldn’t be. The bumping now sounded along the passage outside her room. Selina turned from her satchel to behold a gnome in the doorway. Below, she saw a pair of bowlegs; above, her own little hidebound trunk; between, a broad face, a grizzled beard, a lacklustre eye in a weather-beaten countenance.

===Chinese===
「*Og heden!*（荷兰语：哎呀天哪！）」玛尔切叫起来，慌作一团，双手高举过头。她一阵风跑了，顺带把两条辫子也裹挟而去。楼梯上一阵乱响，还有玛尔切的声音，喊着一句什么，塞莉娜听来像「胡根敦克」（hookendunk）。可她断定不会是这个。这会儿，那咚咚声已经移到她房门外的过道里了。塞莉娜从挎包上转过头，只见门口立着一个小地精。往下看，是一双罗圈腿；往上看，是她自己那口包革的小箱子；中间夹着一张宽脸、一部花白胡子和风霜满面中的一只黯淡的眼睛。

===Original===
“Jakob Hoogendunk,” the gnome announced, briefly, peering up at her from beneath the trunk balanced on his back.

===Chinese===
「雅各布·胡根敦克（Jakob Hoogendunk）。」小地精言简意赅地自报家门，从背上顶得平平正正的箱子底下仰起眼来瞅她。

===Original===
Selina laughed delightedly. “Not really! Do come in. This is a good place, don’t you think? Along the wall? Mr.﻿—Mr. Hoogendunk?”

===Chinese===
塞莉娜欢喜地笑了。「不会吧！快请进。这儿是个好地方，您看？靠墙这儿？呃——胡根敦克先生？」

===Original===
Jakob Hoogendunk grunted and plodded across the room, the trunk lurching perilously above his bowlegged stride. He set it down with a final thump, wiped his nose with the back of his hand﻿—sign of a task completed﻿—and surveyed the trunk largely, as if he had made it. “Thank you, Mr. Hoogendunk,” said Selina, and put out her hand. “I’m Selina Peake. How”﻿—she couldn’t resist it﻿—“how did you leave Rip?”

===Chinese===
雅各布·胡根敦克哼了一声，一路蹬蹬地趟过屋子，那口箱子在他罗圈腿的步子顶上摇摇欲坠。他把箱子咚的一声顿到底，用手背抹了抹鼻子——活计完工的记号——然后大大咧咧地打量那口箱子，仿佛它是他亲手做的。「谢谢您，胡根敦克先生。」塞莉娜说着，伸出手去。「我是塞莉娜·皮克。您来的时候——」她实在忍不住——「里普（Rip）他可好啊？」

===Original===
It was characteristic of her that in this grizzled hired man, twisted with rheumatism, reeking of mould and manure, she should see a direct descendant of those gnarled and bearded bowlers so mysteriously encountered by Rip Van Winkle on that fatal day in the Kaatskills. The name, too, appealed to her in its comic ugliness. So she laughed a soft little laugh; held out her hand. The man was not offended. He knew that people laughed when they were introduced. So he laughed, too, in a mixture of embarrassment and attempted ease, looking down at the small hand extended to him. He blinked at it curiously. He wiped his two hands down his thighs, hard; then shook his great grizzled head. “My hand is all muck. I ain’t washed up yet,” and lurched off, leaving Selina looking rather helplessly down at her own extended hand. His clatter on the wooden stairway sounded like cavalry on a frozen road.

===Chinese===
她的脾性便是如此：在这个叫风湿扭弯了、浑身霉味粪味的老长工身上，她看见的是瑞普·凡·温克尔（Rip Van Winkle）在那个要命的日子里、在卡茨基尔山（Kaatskills）上神秘撞见的那班疙疙瘩瘩、满脸胡子的九柱戏玩手的嫡传后裔。连那个名字，也以它那滑稽的丑怪劲儿讨她喜欢。于是她轻轻笑了两声，把手伸着。那汉子并不见怪。他知道人们受引见的时候总是要笑的。于是他也笑了，一半窘迫，一半努力做出从容，低头看着递到他面前的那只小手。他好奇地朝它眨眨眼。他把两只手顺着大腿狠狠抹了抹；然后摇了摇他那颗大大的花白脑袋。「我的手全是泥。还没洗呢。」说完便一摇一晃地去了，撇下塞莉娜，颇无助地望着自己那还伸着的手。他踩在木头楼梯上的那阵响，像冻硬的路上过了一队骑兵。

===Original===
Left alone in her room Selina unlocked her trunk and took from it two photographs﻿—one of a mild-looking man with his hat a little on one side, the other of a woman who might have been a twenty-five-year-old Selina, minus the courageous jaw-line. Looking about for a fitting place on which to stand these leather-framed treasures she considered the top of the chill drum, humorously, then actually placed them there, for lack of better refuge, from which vantage point they regarded her with politely interested eyes. Perhaps Jakob Hoogendunk would put up a shelf for her. That would serve for her little stock of books and for the pictures as well. She was enjoying that little flush of exhilaration that comes to a woman, unpacking.
