There was about her trunk, even though closed but this very day, the element of surprise that gilds familiar objects when disclosed for the first time in unfamiliar surroundings. She took out her neat pile of warm woollen underwear, her stout shoes. She shook out the crushed folds of the wine-coloured cashmere. Now, if ever, she should have regretted its purchase. But she didn’t. No one, she reflected, as she spread it rosily on the bed, possessing a wine-coloured cashmere could be altogether downcast.

===Chinese===
屋里只剩她一个人了。塞莉娜开了皮箱的锁，从里面取出两张照片——一张是个模样温厚的男人，帽子微微歪戴；另一张是个女人，活像二十五岁的塞莉娜，只是少了那副果敢的下颌线条。她四下里替这两件皮框宝贝觅一个合适的去处，诙谐地想到了那面冷冰冰的鼓的顶盖，后来竟当真把它们摆在了那里，因为再没有更好的避风港；从那个高处，两张照片用彬彬有礼而饶有兴味的眼睛端详着她。也许雅各布·胡根敦克肯替她钉一块搁板。那就可以安置她那一小堆书，也可以安置照片。她正享受着女人开箱解包时的那一阵小小的红晕似的兴奋。她的箱子虽然就在今天才阖上，此刻却自带着一种意外之喜——熟悉的东西头一回在陌生的环境里亮出来，都镀着这样一层金。她取出叠得齐齐整整的一摞保暖羊毛内衣，取出那双结实的鞋。她抖开酒红色开司米压皱的褶子。要说后悔买它，此刻正是时候。可她不悔。她想，一面把它红艳艳地摊在床上，凡是拥有一件酒红色开司米的人，是没有法子太垂头丧气的。

===Original===
The wine cashmere on the bed, the photographs on the drum, her clothes hanging comfortably on wall-hooks with a calico curtain on a cord protecting them, her stock of books on the closed trunk. Already the room wore the aspect of familiarity.

===Chinese===
酒红开司米摊在床上，照片立在鼓顶，衣裳舒舒服服地挂在墙钩上，一幅拴在绳上的印花布帘子挡着灰，一小摞书放在阖拢的箱盖上。这间屋子，已经有了一副熟面孔的模样。

===Original===
From belowstairs came the hiss of frying. Selina washed in the chill water of the basin, took down her hair and coiled it again before the swimmy little mirror over the washstand. She adjusted the stitched white bands of the severe collar and patted the cuffs of the brown lady’s-cloth. The tight basque was fastened with buttons from throat to waist. Her fine long head rose above this trying base with such grace and dignity as to render the stiff garment beautiful. The skirt billowed and puffed out behind, and was drawn in folds across the front. It was a day of appalling bunchiness and equally appalling tightness in dress; of panniers, galloons, plastrons, reveres, bustles, and all manner of lumpy bedevilment. That Selina could appear in this disfiguring garment a creature still graceful, slim, and pliant was a sheer triumph of spirit over matter.

===Chinese===
楼下传来油煎的嘶嘶声。塞莉娜就着盆里的凉水洗了洗，对着脸盆架上那面晃悠悠的小镜子拆散一头头发，又重新盘好。她整了整硬领上缝好的白缎带，拍了拍棕色女装呢的袖口。那件紧身的上衣，从喉咙到腰一排扣子扣得严严实实。她那颗秀气的长头颅在这座严峻的底座上方扬起来，那份优雅端庄，竟把这身僵硬的衣裳都衬美了。裙子后身波浪似的鼓蓬，前身又收出层层褶裥。那是个衣裳上鼓包骇人、紧箍也同样骇人的年代；是裙撑、绦带、胸饰、翻驳、臀垫和一切疙疙瘩瘩、促狭作怪的花样的年代。塞莉娜穿了这身作践人的行头，居然还能显得窈窕、纤柔、轻盈，这实在是精神对物质的彻头彻尾的胜利。

===Original===
She blew out the light now and descended the steep wooden stairway to the unlighted parlour. The door between parlour and kitchen was closed. Selina sniffed sensitively. There was pork for supper. She was to learn that there always was pork for supper. As the winter wore on she developed a horror of this porcine fare, remembering to have read somewhere that one’s diet was in time reflected in one’s face; that gross eating made one gross looking. She would examine her features fearfully in the swimmy mirror﻿—the lovely little white nose﻿—was it coarsening? The deep-set dark eyes﻿—were they squinting? The firm sweet lips﻿—were they broadening? But the reflection in the glass reassured her.

===Chinese===
这会儿她吹熄了灯，走下陡陡的木头楼梯，进了没点灯的客厅。客厅和厨房之间的门关着。塞莉娜敏感地嗅了嗅。晚饭是猪肉。她日后会懂得，晚饭永远是猪肉。随着冬天一天深似一天，她对这猪肉饭食生出一种恐怖，想起不知在哪儿读到过：人吃下去的东西，日子久了总要照到脸上去；吃得粗恶，长相也粗恶。她会对着那面晃悠悠的小镜子提心吊胆地端详自己的五官——那只可爱的小白鼻子——是不是变粗了？那双深陷的黑眼睛——是不是眯细了？那两片结结实实的甜嘴唇——是不是变阔了？好在镜子里的影子总叫她安心。

===Original===
She hesitated a moment there in the darkness. Then she opened the kitchen door. There swam out at her a haze of smoke, from which emerged round blue eyes, guttural talk, the smell of frying grease, of stable, of loam, and of woollen wash freshly brought in from the line. With an inrush of cold air that sent the blue haze into swirls the outer kitchen door opened. A boy, his arm piled high with stove-wood, entered; a dark, handsome sullen boy who stared at Selina over the armload of wood. Selina stared back at him. There sprang to life between the boy of twelve and the woman of nineteen an electric current of feeling.

===Chinese===
她在黑暗里迟疑了片刻。然后她推开厨房门。一团蓝烟似的薄雾朝她涌来，雾里浮出圆圆的蓝眼睛、喉音很重的话语，还有煎油的味、牲口棚的味、沃土的味，以及刚从晾衣绳上收进来的毛料衣裳的味。一股冷气灌进来，卷得那蓝雾直打旋——外面的厨房门开了。一个男孩抱着一摞高过臂弯的炉柴走进来；一个黑黑、英俊、阴郁的男孩，从那抱柴上边瞪着塞莉娜。塞莉娜也瞪着他。这十二岁的男孩和十九岁的女人之间，倏地窜起了一道感觉的电流。

===Original===
“Roelf,” thought Selina; and even took a step toward him, inexplicably drawn.

===Chinese===
「鲁尔夫。」塞莉娜心里想，竟还莫名其妙地被牵着，朝他迈了一步。

===Original===
“Hurry then with that wood there!” fretted Maartje at the stove. The boy flung the armful into the box, brushed his sleeve and coat-front mechanically, still looking at Selina. A slave to the insatiable maw of the wood-box.

===Chinese===
「那柴还不快点儿！」玛尔切在炉边急躁地催。男孩把那抱柴往箱里一撂，机械地掸掸袖子和前襟，眼睛仍望着塞莉娜。柴火箱那张永不餍足的大口的一名奴隶。

===Original===
Klaas Pool, already at table, thumped with his knife. “Sit down! Sit down, teacher.” Selina hesitated, looked at Maartje. Maartje was holding a frying pan aloft in one hand while with the other she thrust and poked a fresh stick of wood into the open-lidded stove. The two pigtails seated themselves at the table, set with its red-checked cloth and bone-handled cutlery. Jakob Hoogendunk, who had been splashing, snorting, and puffing porpoise-fashion in a corner over a hand-basin whose cubic contents were out of all proportion to the sounds extracted therefrom, now seated himself. Roelf flung his cap on a wall-hook and sat down. Only Selina and Maartje remained standing. “Sit down! Sit down!” Klaas Pool said again, jovially. “Well, how is cabbages?” He chuckled and winked. Jakob Hoogendunk snorted. A duet of titters from the pigtails. Maartje at the stove smiled; but a trifle grimly, one might have thought, watching her. Evidently Klaas had not hugged his joke in secret. Only the boy Roelf remained unsmiling. Even Selina, feeling the red mounting her cheeks, smiled a little, nervously, and sat down with some suddenness.

===Chinese===
克拉斯·普尔已经坐上桌，用刀子咚咚地敲桌子。「坐下！坐下，女教师。」塞莉娜迟疑着，看了看玛尔切。玛尔切一手把煎锅高举着，另一手往敞开盖的炉膛里又戳又捅地塞一根新柴。两条辫子已在桌边坐定，桌上铺着红格桌布，摆着骨柄刀叉。雅各布·胡根敦克方才正在墙角对着一小盆水，海豚似的扑腾、哼鼻子、喷气，闹出的动静同盆里那点水全不相称，这会儿也落了座。鲁尔夫把帽子甩上墙钩，坐下。只剩塞莉娜和玛尔切还站着。「坐下！坐下！」克拉斯·普尔又说一遍，满面喜气。「怎么样，卷心菜可好啊？」他呵呵地笑，挤了挤眼。雅各布·胡根敦克哼了一声。两条辫子齐声嗤嗤地笑。炉边的玛尔切也笑了；可冷眼看去，笑得未免有点儿苦。显然，克拉斯并没有把他的笑话私藏着独乐。只有男孩鲁尔夫不笑。连塞莉娜自己也觉出红晕正往颧骨上爬，神经质地微微一笑，有些突兀地坐下了。

===Original===
Maartje Pool now thumped down on the table a great bowl of potatoes fried in grease; a platter of ham. There was bread cut in chunks. The coffee was rye, roasted in the oven, ground, and taken without sugar or cream. Of this food there was plenty. It made Mrs. Tebbitt’s Monday night meal seem ambrosial. Selina’s visions of chickens, oly-koeks, wild ducks, crusty crullers, and pumpkin pies vanished, never to return. She had been very hungry, but now, as she talked, nodded, smiled, she cut her food into infinitesimal bites, did not chew them so very well, and despised herself for being dainty. A slight, distinctive little figure there in the yellow lamplight, eating this coarse fare bravely, turning her soft dark glance on the woman who was making countless trips from stove to table, from table to stove; on the sullen handsome boy with his purplish chapped hands and his sombre eyes; on the two round-eyed, red-cheeked little girls; on the great red-faced full-lipped man eating his supper noisily and with relish; on Jakob Hoogendunk, grazing greedily.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
玛尔切·普尔这会儿往桌上咚咚地摆下一大碗油煎土豆，一盘火腿。面包切成大块。咖啡是黑麦的，炉子里烤过、磨碎，喝时既不放糖也不放奶油。这样的吃食倒管够。它叫特比特太太家的星期一晚饭简直成了神馔仙肴。塞莉娜憧憬中的童子鸡、荷兰油团子、野鸭、脆皮炸麻花和南瓜派，就此烟消云散，一去不返。她本来饿得很，可是此刻，她一面应酬、点头、微笑，一面把吃食切成无穷小的一口口，嚼也没怎么嚼透，又嫌弃自己娇气。黄灯光里一个纤瘦而别致的小小身影，勇敢地吃着这粗粝的饭食，把她柔和的黑眼睛投向那个在炉灶与饭桌之间来来回回无数趟的女人；投向那个阴郁俊俏、两只手皴得发紫、眼神沉沉的男孩；投向那两个圆眼睛红脸颊的小姑娘；投向那个大红脸、厚嘴唇的汉子正香喷喷、响动很大地吃着晚饭；投向雅各布·胡根敦克，正贪婪地啃着。……

===Original===
“Well,” she thought, “it’s going to be different enough, that’s certain.﻿ ﻿… This is a vegetable farm, and they don’t eat vegetables. I wonder why.﻿ ﻿… What a pity that she lets herself look like that, just because she’s a farm woman. Her hair screwed into that knob, her skin rough and neglected. That hideous dress. Shapeless. She’s not bad looking, either. A red spot on either cheek, now; and her eyes so blue. A little like those women in the Dutch pictures Father took me to see in﻿—where?﻿—where?﻿—New York, years ago?﻿—yes. A woman in a kitchen, a dark sort of room with pots of brass on a shelf; a high mullioned window. But that woman’s face was placid. This one’s strained. Why need she look like that, frowsy, harried, old!﻿ ﻿… The boy is, somehow, foreign looking﻿—Italian. Queer.﻿ ﻿… They talk a good deal like some German neighbours we had in Milwaukee. They twist sentences. Literal translations from the Dutch, I suppose.”﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
「好吧，」她想，「这日子可够不一样的了，这是肯定的。……这是座蔬菜农场，可他们自己不吃蔬菜。不知道为什么。……真可惜，就因为自己是农妇，便由着自己弄成那副模样。头发拧成那么个髻，皮肤又粗又没人打理。那身衣裳真难看。没个形。其实她长得并不坏。两颊各有一点红，眼睛又那么蓝。有点像父亲带我去看的那些荷兰画里的女人——在哪儿来着？——在哪儿？——纽约，好多年前？——对了。厨房里的一个女人，一间半明半暗的屋子，架子上一只只黄铜锅；一扇高高的直棂窗。只是那画里的女人一脸安详。这一位却是紧绷绷的。她何苦弄成这样，蓬头垢面、焦头烂额、未老先衰！……那男孩，不知怎么，长得像外国人——像意大利人。怪。……他们说话，挺像我们家在密尔沃基（Milwaukee）时的几个德国邻居。句子都拧着劲儿。想来是荷兰话直译过来的。」……

===Original===
Jakob Hoogendunk was talking. Supper over, the men sat relaxed, pipe in mouth. Maartje was clearing the supper things, with Geertje and Jozina making a great pretense at helping. If they giggled like that in school, Selina thought, she would, in time, go mad, and knock their pigtailed heads together.

===Chinese===
雅各布·胡根敦克在讲话。晚饭既毕，男人们叼着烟斗，浑身松泛地坐着。玛尔切在收拾碗碟，海尔切和约西娜煞有介事地大装帮忙。她们要是在学校里也这么咯咯笑，塞莉娜想，迟早有一天她会疯掉，会把两条辫子的脑袋撞到一起去。

===Original===
“You got to have rich bottom land,” Hoogendunk was saying, “else you get little tough stringy stuff. I seen it in market Friday, laying. Stick to vegetables that is vegetables and not newfangled stuff. Celery! What is celery! It ain’t rightly a vegetable, and it ain’t a yerb. Look how Voorhees he used as much as one hundred fifty pounds nitrate of sody, let alone regular fertilizer, and what comes from it? Little stringy stuff. You got to have rich bottom land.”

===Chinese===
「你得有肥沃的河滩地，」胡根敦克正说着，「不然长出来的就是又小又韧又筋道的玩意儿。星期五我在市场上就见了，摆在那儿。要种就种正经是蔬菜的蔬菜，别种那些新花样。芹菜！芹菜算个啥！它算不得正经蔬菜，也算不得一味草。瞧瞧弗里斯（Voorhees），光硝酸钠就下了足足一百五十磅，更别提普通肥料了，可长出来个啥？又小又筋道的玩意儿。你得有肥沃的河滩地。」

===Original===
Selina was interested. She had always thought that vegetables grew. You put them in the ground﻿—seeds or something﻿—and pretty soon things came popping up﻿—potatoes, cabbages, onions, carrots, beets. But what was this thing called nitrate of soda? It must have had something to do with the creamed cabbage at Mrs. Tebbitt’s. And she had never known it. And what was regular fertilizer? She leaned forward.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜来了兴致。她一向以为蔬菜是自己长的。你把它们埋进地里——种子什么的——不多时东西就冒出来了——土豆、卷心菜、洋葱、胡萝卜、甜菜。可是这个叫硝酸钠的东西又是什么？它一定同特比特太太家的奶油卷心菜有什么干系。她从前竟从不知道。那普通肥料又是什么？她把身子探前去。

===Original===
“What’s a regular fertilizer?”

===Chinese===
「什么叫普通肥料？」

===Original===
Klaas Pool and Jakob Hoogendunk looked at her. She looked at them, her fine intelligent eyes alight with interest. Pool then tipped back his chair, lifted a stove-lid, spat into the embers, replaced the lid and rolled his eyes in the direction of Jakob Hoogendunk. Hoogendunk rolled his slow gaze in the direction of Klaas Pool. Then both turned to look at this audacious female who thus interrupted men’s conversation.

===Chinese===
克拉斯·普尔和雅各布·胡根敦克看了看她。她也看着他们，那双聪慧的秀眼里燃着兴致。普尔于是把椅子往后一翘，掀起一块炉盖，朝火炭里啐了一口，把盖子搁回去，然后眼珠子慢慢滚向雅各布·胡根敦克。胡根敦克把他那迟缓的目光滚向克拉斯·普尔。随后两人一齐转过脸来，打量这个竟敢如此打断男人谈话的大胆女人。

===Original===
Pool took his pipe from his mouth, blew a thin spiral, wiped his mouth with the back of his hand. “Regular fertilizer is﻿—regular fertilizer.”

===Chinese===
普尔从嘴里抽出烟斗，吹出一缕细细的烟圈，用手背抹了抹嘴。「普通肥料就是——普通肥料。」

===Original===
Jakob Hoogendunk nodded his solemn confirmation of this.

===Chinese===
雅各布·胡根敦克庄严地点头，对此深表确认。

===Original===
“What’s in it?” persisted Selina.

===Chinese===
「里头是什么？」塞莉娜追问道。

===Original===
Pool waved a huge red hand as though to waft away this troublesome insect. He looked at Maartje. But Maartje was slamming about her work. Geertje and Jozina were absorbed in some game of their own behind the stove. Roelf, at the table, sat reading, one slim hand, chapped and gritty with rough work, outspread on the cloth. Selina noticed, without knowing she noticed, that the fingers were long, slim, and the broken nails thin and fine. “But what’s in it?” she said again. Suddenly life in the kitchen hung suspended. The two men frowned. Maartje half turned from her dishpan. The two little girls peered out from behind the stove. Roelf looked up from his book. Even the collie, lying in front of the stove half asleep, suddenly ran his tongue out, winked one eye. But Selina, all sociability, awaited her answer. She could not know that in High Prairie women did not brazenly intrude thus on men’s weighty conversation. The men looked at her, unanswering. She began to feel a little uncomfortable. The boy Roelf rose and went to the cupboard in the kitchen corner. He took down a large green-bound book, and placed it in Selina’s hand. The book smelled terribly. Its covers were greasy with handling. On the page margins a brown stain showed the imprint of fingers. Roelf pointed at a page. Selina followed the line with her eye.

===Chinese===
普尔挥动一只巨大的红手，像要轰走这只纠缠不清的虫子。他看了看玛尔切。可玛尔切正摔摔打打地忙她的活。海尔切和约西娜在炉子后头专心玩着她们自己的什么把戏。鲁尔夫坐在桌边看书，一只纤细的手摊开在桌布上，手叫粗活磨得皴裂、沾着泥沙。塞莉娜注意到了——自己却不知道自己注意到了——那手指长而纤细，断裂的指甲薄而细。「可里头到底是什么？」她又说了一遍。霎时间，厨房里的日子悬住了。两个男人皱起眉。玛尔切从洗碗盆边转过半个身子。两个小姑娘从炉后探出头来。鲁尔夫从书上抬起眼睛。连卧在炉前半睡半醒的那条柯利牧羊犬，也突然吐出舌头，眨了一只眼。可塞莉娜一团和气，只管等她要的答案。她无从知道，在高草原，女人是不这样悍然闯进男人们要紧谈话里去的。男人们看着她，不答。她开始觉得有点儿不自在了。男孩鲁尔夫站起身，走到厨房角落的碗柜前。他取下一本绿皮的大书，放到塞莉娜手里。那书气味难闻得很。封皮叫人摸得油光锃亮。书页边上一道道褐渍，显出手指头的印子。鲁尔夫指着一页。塞莉娜顺着那一行看下去。

===Original===
Good Basic Fertilizer for Market-Garden Crops.

Then, below:

- 
						

Nitrate of soda.

- 
						

Ammonium sulfate.

- 
						

Dried blood.

===Chinese===
供商品菜园作物使用的优质基础肥料。

接着，下面：

- 
						

硝酸钠。

- 
						

硫酸铵。

- 
						

干血。

===Original===
Selina shut the book and handed it back to Roelf, gingerly. Dried blood! She stared at the two men. “What does it mean by dried blood?”

===Chinese===
塞莉娜合上书，小心翼翼地递还给鲁尔夫。干血！她瞪着那两个男人。「干血是什么意思？」

===Original===
Klaas answered stubbornly, “Dried blood is dried blood. You put in the field dried blood and it makes grow. Cabbages, onions, squash.” At sight of her horrified face he grinned. “Well, cabbages is anyway beautiful, huh?” He rolled a facetious eye around at Jakob. Evidently this joke was going to last him the winter.

===Chinese===
克拉斯倔头倔脑地答：「干血就是干血。你把干血下到地里，东西就长。卷心菜、洋葱、西葫芦。」见她一脸惊骇，他咧开嘴笑了。「可不是嘛，卷心菜横竖是美的，嗯？」他冲雅各布滑稽地转了转眼珠。显然，这个笑话够他受用一个冬天。

===Original===
Selina stood up. She wasn’t annoyed; but she wanted, suddenly, to be alone in her room﻿—in the room that but an hour before had been a strange and terrifying chamber with its towering bed, its chill drum, its ghostly bride’s chest. Now it had become a refuge, snug, safe, infinitely desirable. She turned to Mrs. Pool. “I﻿—I think I’ll go up to my room. I’m very tired. The ride, I suppose. I’m not used﻿ ﻿…” Her voice trailed off.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜站起身。她并不恼；只是突然想独自回房去——回到那间一个钟头之前还陌生可怖的屋子里去：那张顶天立地的床，那面冰凉的鼓，那口幽灵似的新娘箱。此刻它已经成了一座避难所，温暖、安稳、无限可亲。她转向普尔太太。「我——我想我上楼回房去了。我很累了。大概是坐车的缘故。我不大惯……」她的声音低了下去。

===Original===
“Sure,” said Maartje, briskly. She had finished the supper dishes and was busy with a huge bowl, flour, a baking board. “Sure go up. I got my bread to set yet and what all.”

===Chinese===
「好的。」玛尔切干脆地说。她已收拾完晚饭碗碟，正忙着一应家什：一只大盆、面粉、一块揉面板。「上去吧。我面还没发呢，还有一大堆事儿。」

===Original===
“If I could have some hot water﻿—”

===Chinese===
「要是能给我一点儿热水——」

===Original===
“Roelf! Stop once that reading and show school teacher where is hot water. Geertje! Jozina! Never in my world did I see such.” She cuffed a convenient pigtail by way of emphasis. A wail arose.

===Chinese===
「鲁尔夫！把那书停一停，领女教师看看热水在哪儿。海尔切！约西娜！我这辈子就没见过这样的。」她顺手拍了一下够得着的一条辫子，以壮声势。一声哀号应声而起。

===Original===
“Never mind. It doesn’t matter. Don’t bother.” Selina was in a sort of panic now. She wanted to be out of the room. But the boy Roelf, with quiet swiftness, had taken a battered tin pail from its hook on the wall, had lifted an iron slab at the back of the kitchen stove. A mist of steam arose. He dipped the pail into the tiny reservoir thus revealed. Then, as Selina made as though to take it, he walked past her. She heard him ascending the wooden stairway. She wanted to be after him. But first she must know the name of the book over which he had been poring. But between her and the book outspread on the table were Pool, Hoogendunk, dog, pigtails, Maartje. She pointed with a determined forefinger. “What’s that book Roelf was reading?”

===Chinese===
「不用了。不碍事。别费事。」塞莉娜这会儿竟有点儿着慌。她只想离开这间屋子。可是男孩鲁尔夫已经不出声色、干净利落地从墙钩上摘下一只磕得坑坑洼洼的白铁桶，掀开厨房炉灶后头的一块铁板。一团水汽腾起。他把桶浸进那才露出的小小水槽。随后，塞莉娜做出要接的样子，他却径直从她身边走了过去。她听见他蹬蹬地上了木头楼梯。她想跟上去。可她得先知道他方才埋头苦读的那本书的名字。可是横在她和桌上摊开的那本书之间的，是普尔、胡根敦克、狗、两条辫子、玛尔切。她用一根坚决的食指一指。「鲁尔夫方才念的那本书是什么？」

===Original===
Maartje thumped a great ball of dough on the baking board. Her arms were white with flour. She kneaded and pummelled expertly. “*Woorden boek.*”

===Chinese===
玛尔切把一大团面咚地掼在揉面板上。两条胳膊沾面粉沾得雪白。她又揉又捣，好不老练。「*Woorden boek.*（荷兰语：词书）」

===Original===
Well. That meant nothing. *Woorden boek.* *Woorden b*﻿—Dimly the meaning of the Dutch words began to come to her. But it couldn’t be. She brushed past the men in the tipped-back chairs, stepped over the collie, reached across the table. *Woorden*﻿—word. *Boek*﻿—book. Word book. “He’s reading the dictionary!” Selina said, aloud. “He’s reading the dictionary!” She had the horrible feeling that she was going to laugh and cry at once; hysteria.

===Chinese===
好吧。这话什么意思也没有。*Woorden boek.* *Woorden b*——荷兰字的意思开始隐隐约约地向她走来。可总不会吧。她从往后翘着的椅子上的男人们身边挤过去，跨过那条柯利犬，隔着桌子把身子探过去。*Woorden*——词。*Boek*——书。词书。「他在读词典！」塞莉娜出声说道。「他在读词典！」她有一种可怕的感觉：自己马上要同时笑出来又哭出来了；歇斯底里。

===Original===
Mrs. Pool glanced around. “School teacher he gave it to Roelf time he quit last year for spring planting. A word book. In it is more as a hundred thousand words, all different.”

===Chinese===
普尔太太回头看了一眼。「去年开春下种他不念了，女教师就把这本给了鲁尔夫。一本词书。里头有十多万个词，个个都不一样。」

===Original===
Selina flung a good night over her shoulder and made for the stairway. He should have all her books. She would send to Chicago for books. She would spend her thirty dollars a month buying books for him. He had been reading the dictionary!

===Chinese===
塞莉娜肩膀上甩下一句晚安，便径直奔楼梯去了。她的书全该归他。她要往芝加哥去信买书。她要拿她一个月三十美元的薪水，都给他买书。他一直在读词典！

===Original===
Roelf had placed the pail of hot water on the little washstand, and had lighted the glass lamp. He was intent on replacing the glass chimney within the four prongs that held it firm. Downstairs, in the crowded kitchen, he had seemed quite the man. Now, in the yellow lamplight, his profile sharply outlined, she saw that he was just a small boy with tousled hair. About his cheeks, his mouth, his chin one could even see the last faint traces of soft infantile roundness. His trousers, absurdly cut down from a man’s pair by inexpert hands, hung grotesquely about his slim shanks.

===Chinese===
鲁尔夫已把那桶热水放在小脸盆架上，点亮了那盏玻璃油灯。他正专心致志地把玻璃灯罩安回那四只把它夹稳的灯爪里去。楼下那间挤挤挨挨的厨房里，他俨然是个大人了。此刻，在黄灯光里，他的侧影线条分明，她才看出他不过是个头发蓬乱的小男孩。他的两颊、嘴边、下巴上，甚至还能看出婴儿似的一圈柔圆最后淡淡的余痕。他的裤子，叫一双生手从男人的裤子里改小改出来的，荒唐地挂在他两条细腿上，怪模怪样。

===Original===
“He’s just a little boy,” thought Selina, with a quick pang. He was about to pass her now, without glancing at her, his head down. She put out her hand; touched his shoulder. He looked up at her, his face startlingly alive, his eyes blazing. It came to Selina that until now she had not heard him speak. Her hand pressed the thin stuff of his coat sleeve.

===Chinese===
「他不过是个小男孩。」塞莉娜想，心口倏地一疼。这会儿他正低着头，看也不看她，要从她身边走过去。她伸出手去，碰了碰他的肩膀。他抬起头来看她，那张脸活泛得惊人，一双眼睛灼灼放光。塞莉娜这才意识到，直到此刻，她还没有听他说过一句话。她的手按在他上衣袖子那层薄薄的布上。

===Original===
“Cabbages﻿—fields of cabbages﻿—what you said﻿—they *are* beautiful,” he stammered. He was terribly in earnest. Before she could reply he was out of the room, clattering down the stairs.

===Chinese===
「卷心菜——一片一片的卷心菜地——您说过的——它们*就是*美的。」他结结巴巴地说。他是十二万分的认真。她还没来得及答话，他已经出了屋门，蹬蹬蹬地跑下楼去了。

===Original===
Selina stood, blinking a little.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜站在原地，轻轻眨着眼睛。

===Original===
The glow that warmed her now endured while she splashed about in the inadequate basin; took down the dark soft masses of her hair; put on the voluminous long-sleeved, high-necked nightgown. Just before she blew out the lamp her last glimpse was of the black drum stationed like a patient eunuch in the corner; and she could smile at that; even giggle a little, what with weariness, excitement, and a general feeling of being awake in a dream. But once in the vast bed she lay there utterly lost in the waves of terror and loneliness that envelop one at night in a strange house amongst strange people. She lay there, tensed and tight, her toes curled with nervousness, her spine hunched with it, her leg muscles taut. She peeked over the edge of the covers looking a good deal like a frightened brownie, if one could have seen her; her eyes very wide, the pupils turned well toward the corners with the look of listening and distrust. The sharp November air cut in from the fields that were fertilized with dried blood. She shivered, and wrinkled up her lovely little nose and seemed to sniff this loathsome taint in the air. She listened to the noises that came from belowstairs; voices gruff, unaccustomed; shrill, high. These ceased and gave place to others less accustomed to her city-bred ears; a dog’s bark and an answering one; a far-off train whistle; the dull thud of hoofs stamping on the barn floor; the wind in the bare tree branches outside the window.

===Chinese===
那阵暖着她的心的光，这会儿还在：她在那只不敷使用的小盆里哗啦哗啦地洗；拆下她一头乌黑柔软的浓发；穿上那件肥大的长袖高领睡袍。吹灯之前，她最后望见的一眼，是那面黑鼓像个安分的太监似的守在角落里；这个她还能笑一笑；甚至咯咯地笑出一点儿来——又是疲倦，又是兴奋，又有一股子身在梦中还醒着的迷离。可是一钻进那张大床，她便整个人沉了下去，沉进夜里睡在生人堆里、生房子里会没顶的那阵恐怖与孤独的波涛里。她躺在那里，绷得紧紧的，脚趾紧张地蜷着，脊背紧张地弓着，腿上的肌肉紧张地吊着。她从被子边上偷偷露出眼睛，那模样活像个受惊的小棕仙——倘若有谁看得见的话；眼睛睁得极圆，瞳孔偏向眼角，一副侧耳听又不敢信的神气。十一月凛冽的空气，从那些下过干血肥料的田里刮进来。她打了个寒噤，皱起她那只可爱的小鼻子，仿佛嗅着了空气里这股可憎的浊气。她听着楼下传上来的响动：粗嘎的、听不惯的话音；尖利的、高亢的话音。这些停了，换上另一些她那双城里耳朵更不习惯的声音：一声狗吠和一声应答的狗吠；一列远远的火车汽笛；马蹄砸在谷仓地板上的沉闷的咚咚；窗外光枝秃树里的风声。

===Original===
Her watch﻿—a gift from Simeon Peake on her eighteenth birthday﻿—with the gold case all beautifully engraved with a likeness of a gate, and a church, and a waterfall and a bird, linked together with spirals and flourishes of the most graceful description, was ticking away companionably under her pillow. She felt for it, took it out and held it in her palm, under her cheek, for comfort.

===Chinese===
她的怀表——西缅·皮克在她十八岁生日送的礼物——金表壳上精美地錾着一扇大门、一座教堂、一道瀑布和一只鸟，用最曼妙的螺线和卷草连成一气，此刻正在她枕头底下嗒嗒地、作伴似的走着。她伸手摸到它，掏出来握在掌心里，压在腮帮底下，讨一点安慰。

===Original===
She knew she would not sleep that night. She knew she would not sleep﻿—

===Chinese===
她知道这一夜她是睡不着的。她知道她是睡不着的——

===Original===
She awoke to a clear, cold November dawn; children’s voices; the neighing of horses; a great sizzling and hissing, and scent of frying bacon; a clucking and squawking in the barnyard. It was six o’clock. Selina’s first day as a school teacher. In a little more than two hours she would be facing a whole roomful of round-eyed Geertjes and Jozinas and Roelfs. The bedroom was cruelly cold. As she threw the bedclothes heroically aside Selina decided that it took an appalling amount of courage﻿—this life that Simeon Peake had called a great adventure.

===Chinese===
她一觉醒来，已是十一月一个晴朗清冷的拂晓：孩子们的说话声；马儿的嘶鸣；好大一片滋滋啦啦，一股煎咸肉的香；谷仓院子里咕咕咯咯的鸡叫。六点钟了。塞莉娜当女教师的第一天。再过两个多钟头，她就要面对满满一屋子圆眼睛的海尔切们、约西娜们和鲁尔夫们了。卧室冷得残忍。当她英勇地一把掀开被窝，塞莉娜断定：这需要大得骇人的勇气——这种生活，西缅·皮克叫作一场大冒险的生活。

## IV / 第四章

===Original===
Every morning throughout November it was the same. At six o’clock: “Miss Peake! *Oh*, Miss Peake!”

===Chinese===
十一月里每天早晨都是这一套。六点钟：「皮克小姐！*哎*，皮克小姐！」

===Original===
“I’m up!” Selina would call in what she meant to be a gay voice, through chattering teeth.

===Chinese===
「起来了！」塞莉娜应一声，想装出个欢快的嗓音，牙齿却在格格打战。

===Original===
“You better come down and dress where is warm here by the stove.”

===Chinese===
「你还是下来，这儿炉子边上暖和，就在这儿穿吧。」

===Original===
Peering down the perforations in the floor-hole through which the parlour chimney swelled so proudly into the drum, Selina could vaguely descry Mrs. Pool stationed just below, her gaze upturned.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜从地板上那个小孔格栅的孔眼里望下去——客厅的烟囱正是打这里趾高气扬地鼓胀上去，接进那只「鼓」里——隐约能辨认出普尔太太就守在正下方，仰着脸朝上看。

===Original===
That first morning, on hearing this invitation, Selina had been rocked between horror and mirth. “I’m not cold, really. I’m almost dressed. I’ll be down directly.”

===Chinese===
头一天早晨，听了这番相邀，塞莉娜在骇然与好笑之间来回打摆子。「我不冷，真的。差不多穿好了。这就下来。」

===Original===
Maartje Pool must have sensed some of the shock in the girl’s voice; or, perhaps, even some of the laughter. “Pool and Jakob are long out already cutting. Here back of the stove you can dress warm.”

===Chinese===
玛尔切·普尔准是听出了姑娘话音里的一点骇意，或者竟还有一点笑意。「普尔和雅各布老早就出去砍菜了。炉子后头暖和，你下来穿吧。」

===Original===
Shivering and tempted though she was, Selina had set her will against it. A little hardening of the muscles around her jaw so that they stood out whitely beneath the fine-grained skin. “I won’t go down,” she said to herself, shaking with the cold. “I won’t come down to dressing behind the kitchen stove like a﻿—like a peasant in one of those dreadful Russian novels.﻿ ﻿… That sounds stuck up and horrid.﻿ ﻿… The Pools are good and kind and decent.﻿ ﻿… But I *won’t* come down to huddling behind the stove with a bundle of underwear in my arms. Oh, *dear*, this corset’s like a casing of ice.”

===Chinese===
尽管冻得发抖、又实在眼馋那份暖和，塞莉娜还是横下心来不肯。下巴四周的肌肉绷紧了那么一点儿，在细腻的皮肤底下泛出白来。「我不下去，」她对自己说，冻得直抖。「我才不下去躲在厨房炉子后头穿衣裳，像个——像那些要命的俄国小说里的乡下婆娘。……这话听着又傲慢又刻薄。……普尔一家人是好心人，和善、正派。……可我*就是*不肯下去，抱着一抱内衣挤在炉子边上取暖。哦，*天哪*，这胸衣简直像副冰做的套子。」

===Original===
Geertje and Jozina had no such maidenly scruples. Each morning they gathered their small woollen garments in a bundle and scudded briskly to the kitchen for warmth, though their bedroom just off the parlour had by no means the degree of refrigeration possessed by Selina’s clammy chamber. Not only that, the Misses Pool slept snugly in the woollen nether garments that invested them by day and so had only mounds of woollen petticoats, woollen stockings, and mysterious grimy straps, bands, and fastenings with which to struggle. Their intimate flannels had a cactus quality that made the early martyrs’ hair shirts seem, in comparison, but a fleece-lined cloud. Dressing behind the kitchen stove was a natural and universal custom in High Prairie.

===Chinese===
海尔切和约西娜可没有这等姑娘家的顾忌。每天早晨，她们把小小的绒衣裤卷成一卷，脚步轻快地一溜烟跑进厨房去取暖，虽然她们那间紧挨客厅的卧室，远没有塞莉娜那间阴浸的屋子冷得那么彻底。还不止此，普尔家两位小姐夜里就暖烘烘地穿着白天那身绒线下装睡，于是要对付的，不过是一堆堆羊毛衬裙、羊毛袜子，外加种种神秘的、乌糟糟的带子、条子和系扣。她们贴身的法兰绒衣裳有一股仙人掌的劲儿，两相比较之下，早期殉道者的刚毛苦衣简直算得上绒里衬里的云朵了。在厨房炉子后头穿衣裳，在高草原是天经地义、人人如此的规矩。

===Original===
By the middle of December as Selina stuck her nose cautiously out of the covers into the midnight blackness of early morning you might have observed, if it had been at all light, that the tip of that elegant and erstwhile alabaster feature had been encarmined during the night by a mischievous brush wielded by that same wight who had been busy painting fronds and lacy ferns and gorgeous blossoms of silver all over the bedroom window. Slowly, inch by inch, that bedroom window crept down, down. Then, too, the Pools objected to the icy blasts which swept the open stairway and penetrated their hermetically sealed bedrooms below. Often the water in the pitcher on her washstand was frozen when Selina awoke. Her garments, laid out the night before so that their donning next morning might occupy a minimum of time, were mortuary to the touch. Worst of all were the steel-stiffened, unwieldy, and ridiculous stays that encased the female form of that day. As Selina’s numbed fingers struggled with the fastenings of this iciest of garments her ribs shrank from its arctic embrace.

===Chinese===
到十二月中旬，塞莉娜小心翼翼把鼻子探出被窝、探进清晨那种子夜般的漆黑里去的时候——倘若天色尚有半点亮光，你本可以看出——那只雅致的、先前雪花石膏似的鼻尖，一夜之间已经叫一支促狭的画笔涂上了胭脂红；执笔的正是那个忙了一夜、在她卧室整扇窗上画满叶纹、蕾丝似的羊齿和华丽银花的家伙。那扇卧室窗子慢慢地、一寸一寸地往下爬，越爬越低。再说，那道敞着的楼梯间寒风横扫，连他们楼下关得严丝合缝的卧室也能灌进去，普尔一家人对此也大有意见。塞莉娜醒来时，洗脸台上水罐里的水往往已经冻结成冰。她的衣裳头天夜里就一件件摆好，为的是第二天早晨上身时好省下最短的工夫，摸上去却一股停尸的死冷。最糟的是那种绷着钢条、笨重荒唐的紧身褡，那个年月女性的身子就箍在里头。塞莉娜冻僵的手指跟这件顶顶冰凉的家什的系扣搏斗时，她那几根肋骨直往后缩，躲它北极一般的搂抱。

===Original===
“But I won’t dress behind the kitchen stove!” declared Selina, glaring meanwhile at that hollow pretense, the drum. She even stuck her tongue out at it (only nineteen, remember!). For that matter, it may as well be known that she brought home a piece of chalk from school and sketched a demon face on the drum’s bulging front, giving it a personal and horrid aspect that afforded her much satisfaction.

===Chinese===
「可我就是不在厨房炉子后头穿衣裳！」塞莉娜声明道，一边恶狠狠地瞪着那件虚有其表的家什——那只「鼓」。她甚至朝它吐了吐舌头（别忘了，她才十九岁！）。说起这个，不妨索性都抖搂出来：她从学校带了一截粉笔回家，在那面鼓鼓的前脸上画了个恶魔的脸，给它添了一副别有恩怨的可怕嘴脸，叫她大为得意。

===Original===
When she thought back, years later, on that period of her High Prairie experience, stoves seemed to figure with absurd prominence in her memory. That might well be. A stove changed the whole course of her life.

===Chinese===
多年以后，回想起她在高草原的这段经历，炉子在她的记忆里占着好不成比例的显要地位。这倒也难怪。一只炉子改变了她一生的道路。

===Original===
From the first, the schoolhouse stove was her bête noir. Out of the welter of that first year it stood, huge and menacing, a black tyrant. The High Prairie schoolhouse in which Selina taught was a little more than a mile up the road beyond the Pool farm. She came to know that road in all its moods﻿—ice-locked, drifted with snow, wallowing in mud. School began at half-past eight. After her first week Selina had the mathematics of her early morning reduced to the least common denominator. Up at six. A plunge into the frigid garments; breakfast of bread, cheese, sometimes bacon, always rye coffee without cream or sugar. On with the cloak, muffler, hood, mittens, galoshes. The lunch box in bad weather. Up the road to the schoolhouse, battling the prairie wind that whipped the tears into the eyes, ploughing the drifts, slipping on the hard ruts and icy ridges in dry weather. Excellent at nineteen. As she flew down the road in sun or rain, in wind or snow, her mind’s eye was fixed on the stove. The schoolhouse reached, her numbed fingers wrestled with the rusty lock. The door opened, there smote her the schoolroom smell﻿—a mingling of dead ashes, kerosene, unwashed bodies, dust, mice, chalk, stove-wood, lunch crumbs, mould, slate that has been washed with saliva. Into this Selina rushed, untying her muffler as she entered. In the little vestibule there was a box piled with chunks of stove-wood and another heaped with dried corncobs. Alongside this a can of kerosene. The cobs served as kindling. A dozen or more of these you soaked with kerosene and stuffed into the maw of the rusty iron potbellied stove. A match. Up flared the corncobs. Now was the moment for a small stick of wood; another to keep it company. Shut the door. Draughts. Dampers. Smoke. Suspense. A blaze, then a crackle. The wood has caught. In with a chunk now. A wait. Another chunk. Slam the door. The schoolhouse fire is started for the day. As the room thawed gradually Selina removed layers of outer garments. By the time the children arrived the room was livable.

===Chinese===
打从头一天起，校舍里那只炉子就是她的「黑兽」（bête noir）。在头一年的那片混沌里，它兀立着，庞大，咄咄逼人，一员黑心暴君。塞莉娜教书的那所高草原校舍，在过了普尔农场之后沿路再走一英里出头的地方。这条路的各种脾性她都领教过了——冰封的，积雪成垄的，在烂泥里打滚的。八点半上课。过了头一个星期，塞莉娜就把她清晨的算术化到了最小公分母。六点起身。一头扎进冰冷彻骨的衣裳；早饭是面包、干酪，有时有咸肉，永远是没奶油没糖的黑麦咖啡。披上斗篷，围上围巾，戴上风帽，套上连指手套，蹬上橡胶套鞋。天气坏，再带饭盒。沿路赶去校舍，一路跟草原上的风厮打，风把眼泪直往眼睛里抽；从雪垄里犁过去，天干的日子在硬辙和冰棱上打滑。十九岁干这个，再好没有。她在日头里雨里、风里雪里顺着大路飞奔时，心里的那只眼睛始终盯着炉子。到了校舍，冻僵的手指先跟生锈的锁摔一场跤。门开了，教室那股气味劈头给她一下——死灰、煤油、没洗的身子、尘土、耗子、粉笔、炉柴、饭屑、霉味、叫唾沫擦过的石板，诸般气味浑作一团。塞莉娜一头扎将进去，进门就解围巾。小小门厅里有一口箱子，码满了炉柴块，另一口堆着干玉米芯。旁边一罐煤油。玉米芯是引火用的。拣它十来个，浇上煤油，塞进那口生铁大肚炉的喉咙里。划一根火柴。玉米芯轰地蹿起火苗。这时该上一根细柴了；再来一根给它作伴。关上炉门。风门。闸板。烟。悬心吊胆。一蓬火苗，继而噼啪作响。木头烧着了。现在塞进一块柴。等一等。再一块。砰的关上门。校舍里这一天的火生起来了。屋子慢慢回暖，塞莉娜一层层褪去外衣。等孩子们到的时候，屋里已经住得进人了。

===Original===
Naturally, those who sat near this monster baked; those near the windows froze. Sometimes Selina felt she must go mad beholding the writhings and contortions of a roomful of wriggling bodies scratching at backs, legs, and sides as the stove grew hotter and flesh rebelled against the harsh contact with the prickling undergarments of an overcautious day.

===Chinese===
不用说，挨着这怪物坐的烤得冒油，挨着窗坐的冻得发僵。炉子越烧越热，皮肉造起反来，受不了那过分检点的年代里扎人的内衣贴着皮肉，满屋子扭动的小身子抓背的抓背、挠腿的挠腿、蹭腰的蹭腰，那副辗转腾挪的景象，塞莉娜有时候看着看着，觉得自己非疯掉不可。

===Original===
Selina had seen herself, dignified, yet gentle, instructing a roomful of Dutch cherubs in the simpler elements of learning. But it is difficult to be dignified and gracious when you are suffering from chilblains. Selina fell victim to this sordid discomfort, as did every child in the room. She sat at the battered pine desk or moved about, a little ice-wool shawl around her shoulders when the wind was wrong and the stove balky. Her white little face seemed whiter in contrast with the black folds of this sombre garment. Her slim hands were rough and chapped. The oldest child in the room was thirteen, the youngest four and a half. From eight-thirty until four Selina ruled this grubby domain; a hot-and-cold roomful of sneezing, coughing, wriggling, shuffling, dozing children, toe scuffling on agonized heel, and heel scrunching on agonized toe, in a frenzy of itching.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜本把自己想成端庄而又温柔，给满屋的荷兰小天使讲授入门的学问。可是人害着冻疮的时候，端庄温柔实在难以做到。塞莉娜也着了这道不体面的小罪，屋里哪个孩子也没能幸免。她坐在那张伤痕累累的松木课桌前，或是来回走动，赶上风向不对、炉子使性子，肩上便裹一条小小的冰绒披肩。她那苍白的小脸衬着这件黑衣裳的黑褶子，显得愈发白了。她一双纤手又粗又皴。屋里最大的孩子十三岁，最小的四岁半。从八点半到四点，塞莉娜君临着这片灰扑扑的疆土：一屋子忽冷忽热的孩子，打喷嚏，咳嗽，扭来扭去，蹭蹭沓沓，打瞌睡，脚趾在受罪的脚跟上磨，脚跟在受罪的脚趾上碾，痒得如疯如魔。

===Original===
“Aggie Vander Sijde, parse this sentence: The ground is wet because it has rained.”

===Chinese===
「阿琪·范德赛德（Aggie Vander Sijde），分析这个句子：The ground is wet because it has rained.——地湿了，因为下过雨。」

===Original===
Miss Vander Sijde, eleven, arises with a switching of skirts and a tossing of pigtail. “ ‘Ground’ the subject; ‘is wet’ the predicate; ‘because’﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
范德赛德小姐，十一岁，裙裾一甩、辫子一扬地站起来。「『ground』是主语；『is wet』是谓语；『because』是……」

===Original===
Selina is listening with school-teacherly expression indicative of encouragement and approval. “Jan Snip, parse this sentence: The flower will wither if it is picked.”

===Chinese===
塞莉娜听着，一脸女教师的神情，透着鼓励与嘉许。「扬·斯尼普（Jan Snip），分析这个句子：The flower will wither if it is picked.——花儿要是摘了，就要枯的。」

===Original===
Brown lady’s cloth; ice-wool shawl; chalk in hand. Just a phase; a brief chapter in the adventure. Something to remember and look back on with a mingling of amusement and wonder. Things were going to happen. Such things, with life and life and life stretching ahead of her! In five years﻿—two﻿—even one, perhaps, who knows but that she might be lying on lacy pillows on just such a bleak winter morning, a satin coverlet over her, the morning light shaded by soft rose-coloured hangings. (Early influence of the *Fireside Companion*.)

===Chinese===
棕色女装呢，冰绒披肩，手执一支粉笔。不过是一个阶段；这场历险里短短的一章。一件日后回想起来、滋味里掺着好笑与惊奇的事。好事快要来了。那样的事，而她前头铺展着的，是一程又一程又一程的生命！五年之内——两年——说不定一年，谁知道呢？没准她正躺在花边枕头上，也是这么个凄凉的冬日清晨，身上盖一床缎被，晨光教柔软的玫瑰色帷幔滤得朦朦胧胧。（早年《炉边伴侣》的熏陶。）

===Original===
“What time is it, Celeste?”

===Chinese===
「几点钟了，赛莱斯特（Celeste）？」

===Original===
“It is now eleven o’clock, madame.”

===Chinese===
「现在十一点整，夫人。」

===Original===
“Is that all!”

===Chinese===
「这就才十一点哪！」

===Original===
“Would madame like that I prepare her bath now, or later?”

===Chinese===
「夫人是这就吩咐预备浴呢，还是待一会儿？」

===Original===
“Later, Celeste. My chocolate now. My letters.”

===Chinese===
「待一会儿，赛莱斯特。先上我的巧克力。把我的信拿来。」

===Original===
“… and if is the conjunction modifying﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「……『if』是连词，起修饰作用……」

===Original===
Early in the winter Selina had had the unfortunate idea of opening the ice-locked windows at intervals and giving the children five minutes of exercise while the fresh cold air cleared brains and room at once. Arms waved wildly, heads wobbled, short legs worked vigorously. At the end of the week twenty High Prairie parents sent protests by note or word of mouth. Jan and Cornelius, Katrina and Aggie went to school to learn reading and writing and numbers, not to stand with open windows in the winter.

===Chinese===
入冬不久，塞莉娜出过一个倒霉的主意：隔三差五把冻死的窗子打开，叫孩子们做五分钟体操，好让清冽的冷空气把脑子和屋子一齐换新。胳膊乱挥，脑袋乱晃，短腿使劲蹬踹。到了周末，高草原有二十位家长递了抗议，有递纸条的，有捎口信的。扬和科尼利厄斯，卡特里娜（Katrina）和阿琪，进学校是来学读书写字、学数数的，不是来大冬天开着窗户罚站来的。

===Original===
On the Pool farm the winter work had set in. Klaas drove into Chicago with winter vegetables only once a week now. He and Jakob and Roelf were storing potatoes and cabbages underground; repairing fences; preparing frames for the early spring planting; sorting seedlings. It had been Roelf who had taught Selina to build the schoolhouse fire. He had gone with her on that first morning, had started the fire, filled the water pail, initiated her in the rites of corncobs, kerosene, and dampers. A shy, dark, silent boy. She set out deliberately to woo him to friendship.

===Chinese===
普尔农场上的冬活上来了。克拉斯如今一星期只赶一趟芝加哥送冬菜。他跟雅各布和鲁尔夫把土豆和卷心菜下窖贮存，修篱笆，给开春的栽种备床框，分拣菜苗。教塞莉娜生校舍炉火的正是鲁尔夫。头一天早晨是他陪她去的，生了火，灌满了水桶，把玉米芯、煤油和风闸这一套道道传授给她。一个怕生的、黝黑的、沉默的男孩。她存下心来，定要一步步诱他结成朋友。

===Original===
“Roelf, I have a book called *Ivanhoe*. Would you like to read it?”

===Chinese===
「鲁尔夫，我有一本书，叫《艾凡赫》（*Ivanhoe*）。你想读吗？」

===Original===
“Well, I don’t get much time.”

===Chinese===
「呃，我抽不出多少空。」

===Original===
“You wouldn’t have to hurry. Right there in the house. And there’s another called *The Three Musketeers*.”

===Chinese===
「不用赶的。就在家里读。还有一本，叫《三个火枪手》（*The Three Musketeers*）。」

===Original===
He was trying not to look pleased; to appear stolid and Dutch, like the people from whom he had sprung. Some Dutch sailor ancestor, Selina thought, or fisherman, must have touched at an Italian port or Spanish and brought back a wife whose eyes and skin and feeling for beauty had skipped layer on layer of placid Netherlanders to crop out now in this wistful sensitive boy.

===Chinese===
他竭力不显出高兴来；竭力装得木木讷讷、一副荷兰派头，像他血脉所出的那族人。塞莉娜想，想必是哪位荷兰水手老祖宗——要不就是打鱼的——在意大利或者西班牙的港口靠过岸，带回来一个老婆，她那双眼睛、那身皮肤、那份对美的感应，隔开一层又一层的性情恬静的尼德兰人，到如今在这个怅惘敏感的男孩身上重新冒出头来。

===Original===
Selina had spoken to Jakob Hoogendunk about a shelf for her books and her photographs. He had put up a rough bit of board, very crude and ugly, but it had served. She had come home one snowy afternoon to find this shelf gone and in its place a smooth and polished one, with brackets intricately carved. Roelf had cut, planed, polished, and carved it in many hours of work in the cold little shed off the kitchen. He had there a workshop of sorts, fitted with such tools and implements as he could devise. He did man’s work on the farm, yet often at night Selina could faintly hear the rasp of his handsaw after she had gone to bed. He had built a doll’s house for Geertje and Jozina that was the black envy of every pigtail in High Prairie. This sort of thing was looked upon by Klaas Pool as foolishness. Roelf’s real work in the shed was the making and mending of coldframes and hotbeds for the early spring plants. Whenever possible Roelf neglected this dull work for some fancy of his own. To this Klaas Pool objected as being “dumb.” For that matter, High Prairie considered Pool’s boy “dumb like.” He said such things. When the new Dutch Reformed Church was completed after gigantic effort﻿—red brick, and the first brick church in High Prairie﻿—bright yellow painted pews﻿—a red and yellow glass window, most handsome﻿—the Reverend Vaarwerk brought from New Haarlem to preach the first sermon﻿—Pool’s Roelf was heard to hint darkly to a group of High Prairie boys that some night he was going to burn the church down. It was ugly. It hurt you to look at it, just.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜跟雅各布·胡根敦克提过，想要个搁书和照片的架子。他钉了块毛板子上去，粗陋得很，也难看，倒也将就着用了。一个落雪的下午她回到家，发现那块板子不见了，原处换上一块刨得溜光、磨得发亮的，托架上雕着繁复的花样。那是鲁尔夫在厨房旁边那间冷冰冰的小棚子里，做了好多个钟头的活——锯、刨、磨光、雕刻。他在那儿算有个半拉子工场，一应工具家什，都是他自己设法攒弄出来的。他在农场上干的是大人的活，可是夜里她睡下之后，还常常能隐隐听见他那把手锯的挫磨声。他给海尔切和约西娜盖过一座玩偶小屋，叫高草原上每一条小辫子都眼红得发黑。这类事情，在克拉斯·普尔看来尽是犯傻。鲁尔夫在棚子里的正经活计，是给开春的菜苗做冷床、修温床。只要逮着机会，鲁尔夫就撂下这份闷活儿，去弄他自己那些花样。为这个克拉斯·普尔骂他「呆」。说起来，高草原上公认普尔家这小子「呆得很」。他就是净说这种话。新的荷兰归正会（Dutch Reformed Church）教堂费了九牛二虎之力总算落成——红砖砌的，高草原头一座砖教堂——漆成亮黄色的长椅——一扇红黄两色玻璃的窗子，气派极了——从新哈勒姆（New Haarlem）请来讲首场布道的瓦尔韦克牧师（the Reverend Vaarwerk）——就在这时，有人听见普尔家的鲁尔夫阴沉沉地对一伙高草原男孩放话，说哪天夜里他要放把火，把教堂烧个精光。那教堂丑。瞅一眼都硌得眼睛疼，就这么回事。

===Original===
Certainly, the boy was different. Selina, none too knowledgeous herself, still recognized that here was something rare, something precious to be fostered, shielded, encouraged.

===Chinese===
不错，这孩子是与众不同。塞莉娜自己学问也有限，可她也看得出这里头有样稀罕东西，一样贵重东西，须得培养、护持、勉励。

===Original===
“Roelf, stop that foolishness, get your ma once some wood. Carving on that box again instead finishing them coldframes. Some day, by golly, I show you. I break every stick﻿ ﻿… dumb as a Groningen﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「鲁尔夫，少干那傻事，去给你妈搬点柴火。冷床不收尾，又刻你那个盒子。哪天，走着瞧，我非收拾你不可。把你那些棍儿棒儿一根根全撅折……呆得像个格罗宁根人（Groningen）……」

===Original===
Roelf did not sulk. He seemed not to mind, particularly, but he came back to the carved box as soon as chance presented itself. Maartje and Klaas Pool were not cruel people, nor unkind. They were a little bewildered by this odd creature that they, inexplicably enough, had produced. It was not a family given to demonstration of affection. Life was too grim for the flowering of this softer side. Then, too, they had sprung from a phlegmatic and unemotional people. Klaas toiled like a slave in the fields and barn; Maartje’s day was a treadmill of cooking, scrubbing, washing, mending from the moment she arose (four in the summer, five in the winter) until she dropped with a groan in her bed often long after the others were asleep. Selina had never seen her kiss Geertje or Jozina. But once she had been a little startled to see Maartje, on one of her countless trips between stove and table, run her hand through the boy’s shock of black hair, down the side of his face to his chin which she tipped up with an indescribably tender gesture as she looked down into his eyes. It was a movement fleeting, vague, yet infinitely compassionate. Sometimes she even remonstrated when Klaas berated Roelf. “Leave the boy be, then, Klaas. Leave him be, once.”

===Chinese===
鲁尔夫不怄气。他好像并不怎么往心里去，可只要一得空，转身又回到那只雕花盒子跟前。玛尔切和克拉斯·普尔不是狠心人，也不刻薄。只是对这个他们自己也不知怎么生出来的怪东西，他们有点儿无所适从。这不是一个惯于把亲热摆在明面上的人家。日子太严酷，容不得这一面软处开花。再者，他们的祖上，原就是一支沉滞寡情的族类。克拉斯在地里谷仓里做牛做马；玛尔切的一天是一架踩不完的踏车——做饭、擦洗、浆洗、缝补——从她起身那一刻起（夏天四点，冬天五点），直到她哼哧一声倒在床上，那时节别人往往早睡熟半天了。塞莉娜从没见她亲过海尔切或约西娜。倒有一回，她看见玛尔切在炉灶和饭桌之间数不清的一趟趟奔波之中，把一只手插进男孩那头黑硬的乱发，顺着他的脸颊摸到下巴，用一种难以言传的温柔姿势把那下巴轻轻托起，低头望进他的眼睛里。她不由得微微吃了一惊。那动作一闪即逝，影影绰绰，却怜爱得无边无际。有时克拉斯数落鲁尔夫，她甚至还要拦一拦。「就随这孩子去吧，克拉斯。就随他去这么一回。」

===Original===
“She loves him best,” Selina thought. “She’d even try to understand him if she had time.”

===Chinese===
「她顶疼的是他，」塞莉娜想。「她只要有那个工夫，连懂他都肯去学的。」

===Original===
He was reading her books with such hunger as to cause her to wonder if her stock would last him the winter. Sometimes, after supper, when he was hammering and sawing away in the little shed Selina would snatch Maartje’s old shawl off the hook, and swathed in this against draughty chinks, she would read aloud to him while he carved, or talk to him above the noise of his tools. Selina was a gay and volatile person. She loved to make this boy laugh. His dark face would flash into almost dazzling animation. Sometimes Maartje, hearing their young laughter, would come to the shed door and stand there a moment, hugging her arms in her rolled apron and smiling at them, uncomprehending but companionable.

===Chinese===
他啃她的那些书，啃得那样饥，叫她犯愁这点存货够不够他吃一冬。有时吃过晚饭，他在小棚子里叮叮当当又锤又锯，塞莉娜便从挂钩上抓下玛尔切的旧披肩，拿它裹住自己，挡漏缝里钻进来的风，在他雕刻的当口读书给他听，或者隔着工具的响动跟他说话。塞莉娜是个快活的、心性活络的人。她顶喜欢逗这男孩发笑。他那张黝黑的脸会倏地一亮，亮得几乎晃眼。有时玛尔切听见他们年轻人的笑声，会走到棚子门口站上一会儿，两条胳膊拢在卷起的围裙里，朝他们微笑——不懂，却陪着。

===Original===
“You make fun, h’m?”

===Chinese===
「你们这是找乐子呢，嗯？」

===Original===
“Come in, Mrs. Pool. Sit down on my box and make fun, too. Here, you may have half the shawl.”

===Chinese===
「进来呀，普尔太太。坐我这只箱子上，也一块儿乐呵乐呵。来，披肩分您一半。」

===Original===
“*Og heden!* I got no time to sit down.” She was off.

===Chinese===
「*Og heden!*（荷兰语：哎呀天哪！）我可没工夫坐。」她一阵风去了。

===Original===
Roelf slid his plane slowly, more slowly, over the surface of a satin-smooth oak board. He stopped, twined a curl of shaving about his finger. “When I am a man, and earning, I am going to buy my mother a silk dress like I saw in a store in Chicago and she should put it on every day, not only for Sunday; and sit in a chair and make little fine stitches like Widow Paarlenberg.”

===Chinese===
鲁尔夫把刨子顺着一块缎面般光滑的橡木板推过去，推得很慢，更慢了。他停下来，把一卷刨花绕上手指。「等我成了大人，挣了钱，我要给我妈买一件绸裙子，跟我在芝加哥一家铺子里见过的一样，她得天天穿上身，不光礼拜天穿；还要坐在椅子里，做帕伦贝格寡妇（Widow Paarlenberg）那种细细密密的小针脚。」

===Original===
“What else are you going to do when you grow up?” She waited, certain that he would say something delightful.

===Chinese===
「那你长大了还打算做些什么呀？」她等着，笃定他会说出什么可爱的话来。

===Original===
“Drive the team to town alone to market.”

===Chinese===
「赶起牲口，自个儿进城上市场。」

===Original===
“Oh, Roelf!”

===Chinese===
「哦，鲁尔夫！」

===Original===
“Sure. Already I have gone five times﻿—twice with Jakob and three times with Pop. Pretty soon, when I am seventeen or eighteen, I can go alone. At five in the afternoon you start and at nine you are in the Haymarket. There all night you sleep on the wagon. There are gas lights. The men play dice and cards. At four in the morning you are ready when they come, the commission men and the peddlers and the grocery men. Oh, it’s fine, I tell you!”

===Chinese===
「真的。我已经去过五回了——两回跟雅各布，三回跟我爸。快了，等我到了十七八岁，就能自个儿去了。下午五点动身，九点就到海马克特。整夜就睡在车上。那儿有煤气灯。男人们掷骰子、打牌。清早四点你就候着了，等他们来——掮客啊，小贩啊，杂货铺的伙计啊。哦，那份美气，我跟你说！」

===Original===
“Roelf!” She was bitterly disappointed.

===Chinese===
「鲁尔夫！」她灰心透了。

===Original===
“Here. Look.” He rummaged around in a dusty box in a corner and, suddenly shy again, laid before her a torn sheet of coarse brown paper on which he had sketched crudely, effectively, a melee of great-haunched horses; wagons piled high with garden truck; men in overalls and corduroys; flaring gas torches. He had drawn it with a stub of pencil exactly as it looked to him. The result was as startling as that achieved by the present-day disciple of the impressionistic school.

===Chinese===
「喏，你看。」他在墙角一只积灰的箱子里翻了一气，忽然又害臊起来，把一张撕破的粗棕色纸摆到她面前——纸上是他的素描，笔法粗拙，却极见精神：一群后胯滚圆的马搅作一团；大车装着冒尖的菜蔬；穿工装裤和灯芯绒裤的男人们；熊熊的煤气火炬。他用一截铅笔头，照他眼里的原样一笔笔画下来。那效果，跟如今印象派的门徒弄出来的东西一样叫人吃惊。

===Original===
Selina was enchanted.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜看迷了。

===Original===
Many of her evenings during November were spent thus. The family life was lived in a kitchen blue with pipe smoke, heavy with the smell of cooking. Sometimes﻿—though rarely﻿—a fire was lighted in the parlour stove. Often she had school papers to correct﻿—grubby sheaves of arithmetic, grammar, or spelling lessons. Often she longed to read; wanted to sew. Her bedroom was too cold. The men sat in the kitchen or tramped in and out. Geertje and Jozina scuffled and played. Maartje scuttled about like a harried animal, heavy-footed but incredibly swift. The floor was always gritty with the sandy loam tracked in by the men’s heavy boots.

===Chinese===
十一月里，她许多个夜晚就是这样打发的。一家人的日子都过在厨房里，板烟把屋子熏得发蓝，饭食的气味沉沉压着。有时——难得有一回——客厅炉子里也生上火。她常常有课卷要批——算术、语法、拼写的乱糟糟一叠叠。她常常想读书；想做针线。她的卧室又太冷。男人们坐在厨房里，或者咚咚地进进出出。海尔切和约西娜厮闹着玩。玛尔切像只被撵急了的牲口团团转，脚步沉沉，人却快得叫人不敢信。地板上永远沙沙硌脚，尽是男人们沉重的靴子带进来的沙壤土。

===Original===
Once, early in December, Selina went into town. The trip was born of sudden revolt against her surroundings and a great wave of nostalgia for the dirt and clamour and crowds of Chicago. Early Saturday morning Klaas drove her to the railway station five miles distant. She was to stay until Sunday. A letter had been written Julie Hempel ten days before, but there had been no answer. Once in town she went straight to the Hempel house. Mrs. Hempel, thin-lipped, met her in the hall and said that Julie was out of town. She was visiting her friend Miss Arnold, in Kansas City. Selina was not asked to stay to dinner. She was not asked to sit down. When she left the house her great fine eyes seemed larger and more deep-set than ever, and her jaw-line was set hard against the invasion of tears. Suddenly she hated this Chicago that wanted none of her; that brushed past her, bumping her elbow and offering no apology; that clanged, and shrieked, and whistled, and roared in her ears now grown accustomed to the prairie silence.

===Chinese===
十二月初，塞莉娜进了一趟城。这趟出门，一半是出于对眼面前处境的一阵反叛，一半是一股对芝加哥的尘土、喧嚣和人潮的滔天乡愁。星期六一早，克拉斯赶车送她到五英里外的火车站。她打算住到礼拜天。十天前她给朱莉·亨佩尔写过一封信，却没有回音。一进城，她就直奔亨佩尔家。亨佩尔太太到门厅里见的她，两片薄嘴唇，说朱莉出城去了，在堪萨斯城（Kansas City）她朋友阿诺德小姐家做客。没人留她吃饭。没人请她落座。走出那所房子的时候，她那双秀美的大眼睛显得比往常更大、更深陷，下颌的线条绷得铁硬，抵御着眼泪的进犯。她忽然恨起这芝加哥来——这个不要她的芝加哥；这个擦肩而过、撞了她的胳膊肘也不道歉的芝加哥；这个在她这双听惯了草原寂静的耳朵边铛啷、嘶叫、鸣笛、轰鸣个不停的芝加哥。

===Original===
“I don’t care,” she said, which meant she did. “I don’t care. Just you wait. Some day I’m going to be﻿—oh, terribly important. And people will say, ‘Do you know that wonderful Selina Peake? Well, they say she used to be a country school teacher and slept in an ice-cold room and ate pork three times a﻿ ﻿…’ There! I know what I’m going to do. I’m going to have luncheon and I’ll order the most delicious things. I think I’ll go to the Palmer House where Father and I﻿ ﻿… no, I couldn’t stand that. I’ll go to the Auditorium Hotel restaurant and have ice cream; and chicken broth in a silver cup; and cream puffs, and all kinds of vegetables and little lamb chops in paper panties. And orange pekoe tea.”

===Chinese===
「我不在乎，」她说，意思是很在乎。「我不在乎。你们就等着瞧吧。总有一天我要变得——哦，了不得地重要。人家会说：『你认识那位了不得的塞莉娜·皮克吗？听说呀，她从前当过乡下女教师，睡的是冰窖一样的屋子，一日三餐顿顿吃猪……』瞧见了吧！我知道我要干什么了。我要去吃一顿午饭，专拣顶好吃的东西点。我想去帕尔默旅馆，从前爸爸跟我……不，那儿我受不住。我去大会堂旅馆（Auditorium Hotel）的餐厅，吃冰淇淋；喝银杯盛的清炖鸡汤；吃奶油泡芙，各样蔬菜，还有罩着纸裤衩的小羊排。再喝橙黄白毫（orange pekoe）茶。」

===Original===
She actually did order all these things and had a group of amazed waiters hovering about her table waiting to see her devour this meal, much as a similar group had stared at David Copperfield when he was innocent of having bolted the huge dinner ordered in the inn on his way to London.

===Chinese===
她果真把这些全点了，招来一群咋舌的侍者围着她那张桌子打转，等着看她把这顿饭吞下肚去——恰似当年另一群侍者盯着大卫·科波菲尔（David Copperfield）看那样：那孩子去伦敦路上在客栈里点了满满一大桌酒饭，还浑然不知那桌东西是怎么囫囵下肚的。

===Original===
She ate the ice cream and drank the orange pekoe (mainly because she loved the sound of its name; it made her think of chrysanthemums and cherry blossoms, spices, fans, and slant-eyed maidens). She devoured a crisp salad with the avidity of a canary pecking at a lettuce leaf. She flirted with the lamb chops. She remembered the size of her father’s generous tips and left a sum on the table that temporarily dulled the edge of the waiter’s hatred of women diners. But the luncheon could not be said to have been a success. She thought of dinner, and her spirit quailed. She spent the time between one and three buying portable presents for the entire Pool household﻿—including bananas for Geertje and Jozina, for whom that farinaceous fruit had the fascination always held for the farm child. She caught a train at four thirty-five and actually trudged the five miles from the station to the farm, arriving half frozen, weary, with aching arms and nipped toes, to a great welcome of the squeals, grunts, barks, and gutturals that formed the expression of the Pool household. She was astonished to find how happy she was to return to the kitchen stove, to the smell of frying pork, to her own room with the walnut bed and the book shelf. Even the grim drum had taken on the dear and comforting aspect of the accustomed.

===Chinese===
冰淇淋她吃了，橙黄白毫也喝了（橙黄白毫多半是图它名字的音儿好听；那名字叫她想起菊花和樱花、香料、扇子、细眼睛的少女）。一盘脆生生的凉拌菜，她啄食得像叮着一片生菜叶的金丝雀。小羊排呢，她只跟它们调了一调情。她记起父亲出手赏钱的那份大方数目，也在桌上留了一笔，一时磨钝了那侍者对女食客的仇恨的锋刃。可这顿午饭，怎么说也算不得成功。她一想到晚饭，心里先自怯了。一点到三点，她都用来给普尔全家置备拿得动的礼物——包括给海尔切和约西娜的香蕉，那种面糊糊的水果，对农场孩子永远有的一股魔力。四点三十五分她赶上了火车，出了站，竟当真从车站徒步走了五英里回农场，到家时冻个半僵，乏得发沉，两条胳膊酸痛，脚趾冻得生疼，迎她的是一片大大的欢迎——尖叫声、哼哧声、狗吠声和种种喉音，正是普尔一家表达感情的那套语言。她惊讶地发现自己竟是那样快活：回到了厨房炉子边，回到了煎咸肉的香味里，回到了自己那间有胡桃木床和书架的屋子里。连那只阴森的「鼓」，如今也换上了一副熟稔东西的亲切熨帖的面目。

## V / 第五章

===Original===
High Prairie swains failed to find Selina alluring. She was too small, too pale and fragile for their robust taste. Naturally, her coming had been an event in this isolated community. She would have been surprised to know with what eagerness and curiosity High Prairie gathered crumbs of news about her; her appearance, her manner, her dress. Was she stuck up? Was she new fangled? She failed to notice the agitation of the parlour curtains behind the glittering windows of the farmhouses she passed on her way to school. With no visible means of communication news of her leaped from farm to farm as flame leaps the gaps in a forest fire. She would have been aghast to learn that High Prairie, inexplicably enough, knew all about her from the colour of the ribbon that threaded her neat little white corset covers to the number of books on her shelf. She thought cabbage fields beautiful; she read books to that dumb-acting Roelf Pool; she was making over a dress for Maartje after the pattern of the stylish brown lady’s-cloth she wore (foolishly) to school. Now and then she encountered a team on the road. She would call a good day. Sometimes the driver answered, tardily, as though surprised. Sometimes he only stared. She almost never saw the High Prairie farm women, busy in their kitchens.

===Chinese===
高草原的后生们没觉得塞莉娜有什么动人的地方。对他们结实的口味来说，她太娇小、太苍白、太单薄。自然，她的到来在这片与世隔绝的地方算得一桩大事。倘若知道高草原的人正怀着怎样的热切与好奇，东一星西一点地收罗关于她的消息——她的相貌、她的做派、她的衣裳——她准会大吃一惊。她眼高于顶吗？她爱赶新潮吗？她上学路上打一座座农舍门前经过，竟没留意那些亮堂堂窗户后头客厅窗帘的一阵阵骚动。明明看不见半条传话的渠道，关于她的消息却像山火的火焰蹿过空地似的，从一家农场蹿到另一家农场。高草原的人竟不知怎么对她了如指掌——从穿她那件小巧齐整的白色胸衣罩的缎带是什么颜色，到她书架上有几本书——她若知道，准会骇然。她觉得白菜地美；她给那个呆头呆脑的鲁尔夫·普尔念书；她照着自己（不明智地）穿去学校的那身时髦棕色女装呢的样式，正给玛尔切改一件衣裳。路上她偶尔遇上一挂大车。她会招呼一声日安。有时赶车的迟迟才应一下，仿佛吃了一惊。有时他只管瞪着眼瞧。高草原的农妇们她几乎从来见不着，都忙着在自家厨房里呢。

===Original===
On her fifth Sunday in the district she accompanied the Pools to the morning service at the Dutch Reformed Church. Maartje seldom had the time for such frivolity. But on this morning Klaas hitched up the big farm wagon with the double seat and took the family complete﻿—Maartje, Selina, Roelf, and the pigtails. Maartje, out of her kitchen calico and dressed in her best black, with a funereal bonnet made sadder by a sparse and drooping feather whose listless fronds emerged surprisingly from a faded red cotton rose, wore a new strange aspect to Selina’s eyes, as did Klaas in his clumsy sabbaticals. Roelf had rebelled against going, had been cuffed for it, and had sat very still all through the service, gazing at the red and yellow glass church window. Later he confided to Selina that the sunlight filtering through the crude yellow panes had imparted a bilious look to the unfortunates seated within its range, affording him much secret satisfaction.

===Chinese===
她来到本区的第五个礼拜天，跟着普尔一家上荷兰归正会去做晨间礼拜。玛尔切难得有工夫行这种虚文。可这天早晨，克拉斯套上带双人座的大农车，载了齐全一家人——玛尔切、塞莉娜、鲁尔夫，还有两个小辫子丫头。玛尔切脱下厨房里的印花布衣裳，穿上她顶好的黑衣裳，戴一顶丧仪沉沉的女帽，帽上稀稀落落垂着一茎羽毛，愈发显得悲戚，那蔫头蔫脑的羽尖竟从一朵褪色的红棉布玫瑰里钻出来——在塞莉娜眼里这是一副新鲜的陌生面目，克拉斯穿着他那身笨拙的安息日礼服，也是一样。鲁尔夫起先闹着不肯去，为此挨了揍，做礼拜时从头到尾坐得纹丝不动，眼睛盯着教堂的红黄玻璃窗。后来他向塞莉娜吐露：阳光透过那些粗劣的黄玻璃滤下来，给坐在那片光里的倒霉蛋们染上一副病黄的脸色，叫他暗地里大为受用。

===Original===
Selina’s appearance had made quite a stir, of which she was entirely unaware. As the congregation entered by twos and threes she thought they resembled startlingly a woodcut in an old illustrated book she once had seen. The men’s Sunday trousers and coats had a square stiff angularity, as though chopped out of a block. The women, in shawls and bonnets of rusty black, were incredibly cut in the same pattern. The unmarried girls, though, were plump, red-cheeked, and not uncomely, with high round cheekbones on which sat a spot of brick-red which imparted no glow to the face. Their foreheads were prominent and meaningless.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜的露面引起了不小的轰动，她自己却浑然不觉。会众三三两两往里进的时候，她觉得他们惊人地像她从前见过的一本旧带图图书里的木刻插图。男人们的礼拜天裤子和上衣方方正正、硬邦邦、见棱见角，仿佛是从一整块木头上劈出来的。女人们披着锈黑色的旧披巾、戴着锈黑色的旧女帽，竟不可思议地全是照一个样子裁出来的。倒是那些没出阁的姑娘们，一个个丰满红润，不算不标致，高高的圆颧骨上贴着一搭砖红色，却给脸上添不出半点光彩。她们的额头突出，空落落没有内容。

===Original===
In the midst of this drab assemblage there entered late and rustlingly a tall, slow-moving woman in a city-bought cloak and a bonnet quite unlike the vintage millinery of High Prairie. As she came down the aisle Selina thought she was like a full-sailed frigate. An ample woman, with a fine fair skin and a ripe red mouth; a high firm bosom and great thighs that moved rhythmically, slowly. She had thick, insolent eyelids. Her hands, as she turned the leaves of her hymn book, were smooth and white. As she entered there was a little rustle throughout the congregation; a craning of necks. Though she was bustled and flounced and panniered, you thought, curiously enough, of those lolling white-fleshed and unconventional ladies whom the sixteenth century painters were always portraying as having their toe nails cut with nothing on.

===Chinese===
就在这一群灰扑扑的会众当中，进来一位迟到的、浑身窸窣作响的高个子女人，慢条斯理，披一件城里买来的斗篷，戴一顶跟高草原陈年旧式的帽子全然两样的女帽。她顺着过道款款走来时，塞莉娜觉得她像一艘满帆的三桅快船。一个丰腴的女人，皮肤白皙细洁，一张熟透的红嘴唇；胸脯高高挺实，两条大腿缓慢而有节奏地摆动。她生着厚厚的、傲慢的眼皮。她翻动圣歌集的那双手，又光滑又白。她一进门，全会众起了一阵小小的骚动；一齐伸长脖子。她分明穿着臀垫、荷叶边、裙撑，你却会奇怪地想起那些懒洋洋、白肉身、不拘礼法的仕女们——十六世纪的画家总爱画她们，脚趾甲修得干干净净，身上别的什么也不穿。

===Original===
“Who’s that?” whispered Selina to Maartje.

===Chinese===
「那是谁？」塞莉娜低声问玛尔切。

===Original===
“Widow Paarlenberg. She is rich like anything.”

===Chinese===
「帕伦贝格寡妇。她阔得不得了。」

===Original===
“Yes?” Selina was fascinated.

===Chinese===
「是吗？」塞莉娜听得津津有味。

===Original===
“Look once how she makes eyes at him.”

===Chinese===
「你且瞧她怎么朝他抛媚眼。」

===Original===
“At him? Who? Who?”

===Chinese===
「朝他？谁？谁呀？」

===Original===
“Pervus DeJong. By Gerrit Pon he is sitting with the blue shirt and sad looking so.”

===Chinese===
「珀维斯·德容。坐在格里特·庞（Gerrit Pon）旁边那个，穿蓝衬衫、一脸愁相的。」

===Original===
Selina craned, peered. “The﻿—oh﻿—he’s very good looking, isn’t he?”

===Chinese===
塞莉娜伸长脖子，眯眼细瞧。「那——哦——他长得很俊，是不是？」

===Original===
“Sure. Widow Paarlenberg is stuck on him. See how she﻿—Sh-sh-sh!﻿—Reverend Dekker looks at us. I tell you after.”

===Chinese===
「可不。帕伦贝格寡妇迷上他啦。你瞧她怎么——嘘——嘘！德克尔牧师（Reverend Dekker）看咱们了。回头我再告诉你。」

===Original===
Selina decided she’d come to church oftener. The service went on, dull, heavy. It was in English and Dutch. She heard scarcely a word of it. The Widow Paarlenberg and this Pervus DeJong occupied her thoughts. She decided, without malice, that the widow resembled one of the sleekest of the pink porkers rooting in Klaas Pool’s barnyard, waiting to be cut into Christmas meat.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜打定主意往后要常来做礼拜。礼拜往下进行，沉闷，滞重。一半英语，一半荷兰语。她几乎没有听进一个字。帕伦贝格寡妇和这个珀维斯·德容占满了她的心思。她并无恶意地断定，这位寡妇活像克拉斯·普尔家场院里拱着土的粉红猪崽里顶油光水滑的一头，正等着挨刀做圣诞肉呢。

===Original===
The Widow Paarlenberg turned and smiled. Her eyes were slippery (Selina’s term). Her mouth became loose and wide with one corner sliding down a trifle into something very like a leer.

===Chinese===
帕伦贝格寡妇回过头来，嫣然一笑。她那双眼睛滑溜溜的（塞莉娜的说法）。她的嘴松松地咧大，一边嘴角微微往下一塌，塌出一副很像是斜睨讪笑的神情。

===Original===
With one surge the Dutch Reformed congregation leaned forward to see how Pervus DeJong would respond to this public mark of favour. His gaze was stern, unsmiling. His eyes were fixed on that extremely dull gentleman, the Reverend Dekker.

===Chinese===
荷兰归正会的会众齐刷刷往前一倾，都要看珀维斯·德容怎样领受这份当众的抬举。他的目光严厉，不带笑意。眼睛直勾勾钉在那位乏味透顶的先生身上——德克尔牧师。

===Original===
“He’s annoyed,” thought Selina, and was pleased at the thought. “Well, I may not be a widow, but I’m sure that’s not the way.” And then: “Now I wonder what it’s like when *he* smiles.”

===Chinese===
「他不耐烦了，」塞莉娜想道，想到这里心头一快。「哼，我又不是寡妇，可我敢保这不是个路数。」接着又想：「不知道*他*笑起来是什么模样。」

===Original===
According to fiction as Selina had found it in the *Fireside Companion* and elsewhere, he should have turned at this moment, irresistibly drawn by the magnetism of her gaze, and smiled a rare sweet smile that lighted up his stern young face. But he did not. He yawned suddenly and capaciously. The Reformed Dutch congregation leaned back feeling cheated. Handsome, certainly, Selina reflected. But then, probably Klaas Pool, too, had been handsome a few years ago.

===Chinese===
照塞莉娜在《炉边伴侣》一类小说里读来的路数，此刻他该转过身来，被她目光的磁力身不由己地吸住，绽出一丝难得的甜笑，照亮他那张严厉的年轻的脸。可是他没有。他突然打了个哈欠，打得又深又宏。归正会的荷兰会众们往后一靠，都觉得上了当。俊，自然是真俊，塞莉娜寻思。不过话说回来，几年前克拉斯·普尔八成也俊过。

===Original===
The service ended, there was much talk of the weather, seedlings, stock, the approaching holiday season. Maartje, her Sunday dinner heavy on her mind, was elbowing her way up the aisle. Here and there she introduced Selina briefly to a woman friend. “Mrs. Vander Sijde, meet school teacher.”

===Chinese===
礼拜结束，大家大谈天气、秧苗、牲口，还有眼看就到的节下。玛尔切心里压着礼拜天的正餐，正沿着过道往门口挤。走一路，把塞莉娜草草介绍给相熟的女客。「范德赛德太太（Mrs. Vander Sijde），见见女教师。」

===Original===
“Aggie’s mother?” Selina would begin, primly, only to be swept along by Maartje on her way to the door. “Mrs. Von Mijnen, meet school teacher. Is Mrs. Von Mijnen.” They regarded her with a grim gaze. Selina would smile and nod rather nervously, feeling young, frivolous, and somehow guilty.

===Chinese===
「阿琪的妈妈吗？」塞莉娜往往端端正正地开口问，话没问完人已被玛尔切裹挟着往门口去了。「冯·迈宁太太（Mrs. Von Mijnen），见见女教师。这位是冯·迈宁太太。」她们用一种冷冷的目光打量她。塞莉娜便笑笑，点点头，多少有点儿局促，觉得自己年轻、轻浮，不知怎么还有点心虚。

===Original===
When, with Maartje, she reached the church porch Pervus DeJong was unhitching the dejected horse that was harnessed to his battered and lopsided cart. The animal stood with four feet bunched together in a drooping and pathetic attitude and seemed inevitably meant for mating with this decrepit vehicle. DeJong untied the reins quickly, and was about to step into the sagging conveyance when the Widow Paarlenberg sailed down the church steps with admirable speed for one so amply proportioned. She made straight for him, skirts billowing, flounces flying, plumes waving. Maartje clutched Selina’s arm. “Look how she makes! She asks him to eat Sunday dinner I bet you! See once how he makes with his head no.”

===Chinese===
她跟着玛尔切走到教堂门廊时，珀维斯·德容正在解他那匹蔫头耷脑的马，马套着一辆破破烂烂、歪歪斜斜的马车。那牲口四只蹄子并作一撮站着，垂头丧气，一副可怜相，仿佛天生就命定配这辆老朽的破车。德容利利索索解开缰绳，正要跨上这辆塌了腰的车，帕伦贝格寡妇以她那样富态的身量来说惊人利落的速度，一阵风掠下教堂台阶。她径直朝他冲去，裙裾鼓荡，荷叶边翻飞，帽羽乱颤。玛尔切一把攥住塞莉娜的胳膊。「你瞧她那架势！我打赌她请他吃礼拜天的饭！你瞧他怎么摇头、怎么摇头。」

===Original===
Selina﻿—and the whole congregation unashamedly watching﻿—could indeed see how he made with his head no. His whole body seemed set in negation﻿—the fine head, the broad patient shoulders, the muscular powerful legs in their ill-fitting Sunday blacks. He shook his head, gathered up the reins, and drove away, leaving the Widow Paarlenberg to carry off with such bravado as she could muster this public flouting in full sight of the Dutch Reformed congregation of High Prairie. It must be said that she actually achieved this feat with a rather magnificent composure. Her round pink face, as she turned away, was placid; her great cowlike eyes mild. Selina abandoned the pink porker simile for that of a great Persian cat, full-fed and treacherous, its claws all sheathed in velvet. The widow stepped agilely into her own neat phaeton with its sleek horse and was off down the hard snowless road, her head high.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜——还有毫不加掩饰地围观着的全体会众——确实瞧见了他是怎么摇头的。他整个身子似乎都拗在一个「不」字上——那颗好看的头颅，那副宽厚坚忍的肩膀，那两条裹在不合身的黑色礼拜天裤子里虬筋盘结的腿。他摇了摇头，拢起缰绳，赶车走了，撇下帕伦贝格寡妇当着高草原荷兰归正会全体会众的面受这一场公开的挫辱，只得搜罗起全身的虚劲硬撑场面。必须说，这份功夫她还真使得相当雍容。她转身走开时，那张团团的粉红脸盘平平静静；那双母牛似的大眼睛温温和和。塞莉娜放弃了粉红猪崽的比方，换成一整天吃撑了的大波斯猫——奸刁叵测，爪子尽数收在天鹅绒鞘里。寡妇轻捷地登上自己那辆整洁的轻便四轮马车，驾着油光水滑的骏马，顺着坚硬的无雪道路驶去了，头昂得高高的。

===Original===
“Well!” exclaimed Selina, feeling as though she had witnessed the first act of an exciting play. And breathed deeply. So, too, did the watching congregation, so that the widow could be said to have driven off in quite a gust.

===Chinese===
「哎呀！」塞莉娜喊出声来，只觉得像看完了紧张好戏的第一幕。她深深吸了口气。围观的会众也深深吸了口气，于是不妨说，寡妇是在一阵风的叹息里驶走的。

===Original===
As they jogged home in the Pool farm wagon Maartje told her tale with a good deal of savour.

===Chinese===
普尔家农车吱吱嘎嘎往家走的路上，玛尔切把这段故事讲得有滋有味。

===Original===
Pervus DeJong had been left a widower two years before. Within a month of that time Leendert Paarlenberg had died, leaving to his widow the richest and most profitable farm in the whole community. Pervus DeJong, on the contrary, through inheritance from his father, old Johannes, possessed a scant twenty-five acres of the worst lowland﻿—practically the only lowland﻿—in all High Prairie. The acreage was notoriously barren. In spring, the critical time for seedlings and early vegetable crops, sixteen of the twenty-five were likely to be under water. Pervus DeJong patiently planted, sowed, gathered crops, hauled them to market; seemed still never to get on in this thrifty Dutch community where getting on was so common a trait as to be no longer thought a virtue. Luck and nature seemed to work against him. His seedlings proved unfertile; his stock was always ailing; his cabbages were worm-infested; snout-beetle bored his rhubarb. When he planted largely of spinach, hoping for a wet spring, the season was dry. Did he turn the following year to sweet potatoes, all auguries pointing to a dry spring and summer, the summer proved the wettest in a decade. Insects and fungi seemed drawn to his fields as by a malevolent force. Had he been small, puny, and insignificant his bad luck would have called forth contemptuous pity. But there was about him the lovableness and splendour of the stricken giant. To complete his discomfort, his household was inadequately ministered by an elderly and rheumatic female connection whose pies and bread were the scandal of the neighbouring housewives.

===Chinese===
珀维斯·德容两年前死了老婆。不出一个月，伦德特·帕伦贝格（Leendert Paarlenberg）也咽了气，把全乡顶肥、顶出息的一座农场留给寡妇。珀维斯·德容正相反，从父亲老约翰内斯（Johannes）手里继承到的，是区区二十五英亩全高草原顶坏的洼地——简直是高草原仅有的洼地。那片地贫得出了名。到了春天——正是秧苗和早菜要命的当口——二十五英亩里倒有十六英亩多半泡在水底下。珀维斯·德容耐心地栽，种，收，把菜拉去上市；在这个勤俭的荷兰人乡里——在这里「起家」是人人皆有的本领，早就算不得什么德行——他却好像永远起不来。运气和老天爷都像是跟他作对。他的秧苗不出息；他的牲口常年在闹病；他的白菜尽生虫；象鼻虫蛀他的大黄。他大种菠菜，指望一个多雨的春天，那年偏偏旱。第二年改种红薯，种种征候都指着春夏要旱，那年夏天偏偏是十年里顶涝的一个。虫子和霉菌像受了一股邪劲招引似的，专往他的地里聚。他若是生得矮小、孱弱、不起眼，他这霉运倒会招来几分带轻蔑的怜悯。可他身上偏偏有一种遭了雷殛的巨人的可爱与堂皇。雪上加霜的是，他家里只由一位上了年纪又害风湿的女亲戚勉强操持着，那位做的馅饼和面包，在左近的主妇们口中是桩丑闻。

===Original===
It was on this Pervus DeJong, then, that the Widow Paarlenberg of the rich acres, the comfortable farmhouse, the gold neck chain, the silk gowns, the soft white hands and the cooking talents, had set her affections. She wooed him openly, notoriously, and with a Dutch vehemence that would have swept another man off his feet. It was known that she sent him a weekly baking of cakes, pies, and bread. She urged upon him choice seeds from her thriving fields; seedlings from her hotbeds; plants, all of which he steadfastly refused. She tricked, cajoled, or nagged him into eating her ample meals. She even asked his advice﻿—that subtlest form of flattery. She asked him about sub-soiling, humus, rotation﻿—she whose rich land yielded, under her shrewd management, more profitably to the single acre than to any ten of Pervus’s. One Jan Bras managed her farm admirably under her supervision.

===Chinese===
于是，冲着这珀维斯·德容，这位坐拥良田、宽宅大院、金项链、绸衣裳、白嫩小手和一手好烹调的帕伦贝格寡妇，倾尽了她的柔情。她追他追得公开露骨、满城风雨，那股荷兰式的烈火劲儿，换个男人早叫燎得晕头转向。人尽皆知，她每礼拜给他送去一炉糕点、馅饼加面包。她把自家兴旺地里精选的种子往他手里推；温床里育的秧；花花草草——一概叫他一口回绝。她连哄带骗、连磨带逼，非要他吃她那些丰盛的饭食。她甚至向他讨教——那是奉承里最精微的一路。她问他心土深耕、腐殖土、轮作——她那片沃土在她精明的经营下，单亩的出息赛过珀维斯随便哪十亩。她手下有个扬·布拉斯（Jan Bras），在她监督之下把她的农场料理得头头是道。

===Original===
DeJong’s was a simple mind. In the beginning, when she said to him, in her deep, caressing voice, “Mr. DeJong, could I ask you a little advice about something? I’m a woman alone since I haven’t got Leendert any more, and strangers what do they care how they run the land! It’s about my radishes, lettuce, spinach, and turnips. Last year, instead of tender, they were stringy and full of fibre on account that Jan Bras. He’s for slow growing. Those vegetables you’ve got to grow quick. Bras says my fertilizer is the fault, but I know different. What you think?”

===Chinese===
德容是个头脑简单的人。起先，她用她那深沉温存的嗓音对他说：「德容先生，有桩事我能跟你讨个主意吗？我一个女人家，自打没了伦德特就孤身一人，外人种地哪管你把地怎么糟践！是为着我的萝卜、生菜、菠菜和芜菁。去年一年，种出来不是嫩生生，反倒筋多丝老、尽是粗纤维，都为着那个扬·布拉斯。他主张慢慢长。这些菜可得长得快。布拉斯说是我的肥料不对，我知道不是。你说呢？」

===Original===
Jan Bras, getting wind of this, told it abroad with grim humour. Masculine High Prairie, meeting Pervus DeJong on the road, greeted him with: “Well, DeJong, you been giving the Widow Paarlenberg any good advice here lately about growing?”

===Chinese===
扬·布拉斯得了风声，把它添油加醋地散布出去，一脸冷面诙谐。高草原的爷儿们路上撞见珀维斯·德容，就招呼道：「嘿，德容，近来给帕伦贝格寡妇出了什么种地的好主意没有哇？」

===Original===
It had been a particularly bad season for his fields. As High Prairie poked a sly thumb into his ribs thus he realized that he had been duped by the wily widow. A slow Dutch wrath rose in him against her; a male resentment at being manipulated by a woman. When next she approached him, cajolery in her voice, seeking guidance about tillage, drainage, or crops, he said, bluntly: “Better you ask Harm Tien his advice.” Harm Tien was the district idiot, a poor witless creature of thirty with the mind of a child.

===Chinese===
这一年他的地里格外不顺。高草原这样伸着促狭的拇指戳他的肋条，他才醒过闷儿来：自己叫那奸猾的寡妇耍了。一股慢火燎的荷兰式怒气冲着她升上来；一股叫女人玩弄于股掌之上的男子汉的怨愤。等她下一次凑上来，嗓音里带着蜜，问他耕作、排水或是茬口的事，他就直愣愣地说：「你还是去讨哈姆·蒂恩（Harm Tien）的主意吧。」哈姆·蒂恩是本区的白痴，三十岁的可怜虫，一副孩童的心智。

===Original===
Knowing well that the entire community was urging him toward this profitable match with the plump, rich, red-lipped widow, Pervus set his will like a stubborn steer and would have none of her. He was uncomfortable in his untidy house; he was lonely, he was unhappy. But he would have none of her. Vanity, pride, resentment were all mixed up in it.

===Chinese===
珀维斯明知道全乡上上下下都在撺掇他结这门肥亲——跟那丰满、有钱、红嘴唇的寡妇——他却像头犟牛似的把心一横，不要她。他待在自己邋遢的屋子里不自在；他孤单，他闷闷不乐。可他就是不要她。虚荣、骄傲、怨愤，全都搅和在这一锅里。

===Original===
The very first time that Pervus DeJong met Selina he had a chance to protect her. With such a start, the end was inevitable. Then, too, Selina had on the wine-coloured cashmere and was trying hard to keep the tears back in full view of the whole of High Prairie. Urged by Maartje (and rather fancying the idea) Selina had attended the great meeting and dance at Adam Ooms’s hall above the general store near the High Prairie station. Farmer families for miles around were there. The new church organ﻿—that time-hallowed pretext for sociability﻿—was the excuse for this gathering. There was a small admission charge. Adam Ooms had given them the hall. The three musicians were playing without fee. The women were to bring supper packed in boxes or baskets, these to be raffled off to the highest bidder whose privilege it then was to sup with the fair whose basket he had bought. Hot coffee could be had at so much the cup. All the proceeds were to be devoted to the organ. It was understood, of course, that there was to be no lively bidding against husbands. Each farm woman knew her own basket as she knew the countenance of her children, and each farmer, as that basket came up at auction, named a cautious sum which automatically made him the basket’s possessor. The larger freedom had not come to High Prairie in 1890. The baskets and boxes of the unwed women were to be the fought-for prizes. Maartje had packed her own basket at noon and had driven off at four with Klaas and the children. She was to serve on one of those bustling committees whose duties ranged from coffee making to dish washing. Klaas and Roelf were to be pressed into service. The pigtails would slide up and down the waxed floor of Ooms’s hall with other shrieking pigtails of the neighbourhood until the crowd began to arrive for the auction and supper. Jakob Hoogendunk would convey Selina to the festivities when his chores were done. Selina’s lunch basket was to be a separate and distinct affair, offered at auction with those of the Katrinas and Linas and Sophias of High Prairie. Not a little apprehensive, she was to pack this basket herself. Maartje, departing, had left copious but disjointed instructions.

===Chinese===
珀维斯·德容头一回见到塞莉娜，就得了个保护她的机会。有这样一个开头，结局便注定了。何况那回塞莉娜正穿着那件酒红色开司米，正使足了劲把眼泪憋回去，而且是在高草原全体人马的眼皮子底下。是玛尔切撺掇她去的（她自己也有几分喜欢这主意）：高草原车站附近那家杂货铺楼上，亚当·奥姆斯（Adam Ooms）的厅堂里有一场大聚会兼舞会。方圆几英里的农家都到了。教堂新添的管风琴——那件年深日久、专供社交借用的名目——正是这次聚会的由头。入场收一点费。厅堂是亚当·奥姆斯捐的。三个乐师分文不取奏乐。女人们把晚饭装在盒子或篮子里带来，这些要拿出来竞卖，价高者得，谁买到哪位姑娘的篮子，谁便有福气同她共进晚餐。热咖啡论杯另算钱。全部收入都归到风琴名下。当然，大家心照不宣，跟人家做丈夫的不兴起劲地抬价。每个农妇认得自己的篮子，如同认得自己孩子的脸；每个农夫，轮到自家篮子上场，便报出一个谨慎的数目，顺理成章成了篮子的主人。更大程度的自由，在1890年还没有传到高草原。要争要抢的，是没出阁的姑娘们的篮子盒子。玛尔切晌午就把自己的篮子装好，四点钟带着克拉斯和孩子们先赶了过去。她要在那种忙忙乱乱的委员会里当差，职司从煮咖啡一路排到洗碗。克拉斯和鲁尔夫要被抓差。两个小辫子丫头要跟街坊四邻别的小辫子丫头一道，在奥姆斯厅堂打过蜡的地板上出溜出溜滑上滑下，直到竞卖和吃晚饭的人群开始进场。雅各布·胡根敦克做完牲口棚里的活计，就送塞莉娜去赴会。塞莉娜的午饭篮是单另的一件，要跟高草原的卡特里娜们、莉娜们、索菲娅们的一起竞卖。她不免有点儿提心吊胆，这只篮子得她亲手来装。玛尔切临走丢下一大堆颠三倒四的嘱咐。

===Original===
“Ham﻿ ﻿… them big cookies in the crock﻿ ﻿… pickles﻿ ﻿… watch how you don’t spill﻿ ﻿… plum preserves﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「火腿……瓦罐里那些大块儿的饼干……腌黄瓜……当心别撒了……李子酱……」

===Original===
Maartje’s own basket was of gigantic proportions and staggering content. Her sandwiches were cubic blocks; her pickles clubs of cucumber; her pies vast plateaus.

===Chinese===
玛尔切自己的篮子大得没了边儿，内容骇人。她的三明治是方方正正的砖块；她的腌黄瓜是根根黄瓜棒槌；她的馅饼是一片片高原。

===Original===
The basket provided for Selina, while not quite so large, still was of appalling size as Selina contemplated it. She decided, suddenly, that she would have none of it. In her trunk she had a cardboard box such as shoes come in. Certainly this should hold enough lunch for two, she thought. She and Julie Hempel had used such boxes for picnic lunches on their Saturday holidays. She was a little nervous about the whole thing; rather dreaded the prospect of eating her supper with a High Prairie swain unknown to her. Suppose no one should bid for her box! She resolved to fill it after her own pattern, disregarding Maartje’s heavy provender.

===Chinese===
给塞莉娜备下的那只篮子虽说没有这么大，塞莉娜端详起来，尺寸也够吓人。她突然拿定主意，不要它。她的大箱子里有一只纸板盒，就是装鞋的那种。两个人的一顿晚饭总装得下，她想。从前礼拜六放假，她和朱莉·亨佩尔野餐就用这种盒子装午饭。她对整桩事有点儿心虚；一想到要跟哪位素不相识的高草原后生共进晚饭，更有点怕。万一她的盒子没人出价呢！她打定主意照自己的路数来装，把玛尔切那份粗重吃食撇在一边。

===Original===
She had the kitchen to herself. Jakob was in the fields or outhouses. The house was deliciously quiet. Selina rummaged for the shoe box, lined it with a sheet of tissue paper, rolled up her sleeves, got out mixing bowl, flour, pans. Cup cakes were her ambition. She baked six of them. They came out a beautiful brown but somewhat leaden. Still, anything was better than a wedge of soggy pie, she told herself. She boiled eggs very hard, halved them, devilled their yolks, filled the whites neatly with this mixture and clapped the halves together again, skewering them with a toothpick. Then she rolled each egg separately in tissue paper twisted at the ends. Daintiness, she had decided, should be the keynote of her supper box. She cut bread paper-thin and made jelly sandwiches, scorning the ubiquitous pork. Bananas, she knew, belonged in a lunch box, but these were unobtainable. She substituted two juicy pippins, polished until their cheeks glittered. The food neatly packed she wrapped the box in paper and tied it with a gay red ribbon yielded by her trunk. At the last moment she whipped into the yard, twisted a brush of evergreen from the tree at the side of the house, and tucked this into the knot of ribbon atop the box. She stepped back and thought the effect enchanting.

===Chinese===
厨房里只有她一个人。雅各布下地或上棚圈去了。屋里静得沁人心脾。塞莉娜翻出那只鞋盒，垫上一层薄棉纸，挽起袖子，取出和面盆、面粉、烤盘。她的抱负是杯形小蛋糕。她烤了六个。出炉颜色焦黄好看，只是有点死沉死沉。不过，她宽慰自己，怎么也强似一角皮塌塌的湿馅饼。她把鸡蛋煮得老老的，一切两半，蛋黄掏出来拌成香辣的馅，整整齐齐填回蛋白窝里，再把两半对扣起来，拿牙签别住。然后每只蛋单独裹一层薄棉纸，两头一拧。雅致——她早已定夺——该是她这只晚饭盒子的基调。她把面包切成纸一样的薄片，做果酱三明治，看不上那无处不在的猪肉。香蕉么，她知道该进午饭盒，可是弄不着。她改放两只水灵灵的香苹果，擦得苹果腮儿亮光闪闪。吃食装停当，她拿纸把盒子包好，从箱子里翻出一根喜庆的红缎带扎上。临了她一溜烟跑进院子，从屋侧那棵树上拧下一小枝常青，插进盒顶缎带打的结里。她退后一步端详，只觉得这效果迷人极了。

===Original===
She was waiting in her red cashmere and her cloak and hood when Hoogendunk called for her. They were late arrivals, for outside Ooms’s hall were hitched all manner of vehicles. There had been a heavy snowfall two days before. This had brought out bobsleds, cutters, sleighs. The horse sheds were not large enough to shelter all. Late comers had to hitch where they could. There was a great jangling of bells as the horses stamped in the snow.

===Chinese===
胡根敦克来接她时，她已穿好红开司米，罩上斗篷，戴好风帽等着。他们到得算晚，奥姆斯厅堂外头已经拴满了各式各样的车辆。前两天下过一场大雪。这场雪把长雪橇、单马雪车、双马拉橇全引出来了。马棚不够大，容不下。晚来的只好见空就拴。马在雪地里刨着蹄子，铃铛乱响成一片。

===Original===
Selina, balancing her box carefully, opened the door that led to the wooden stairway. The hall was on the second floor. The clamour that struck her ears had the effect of a physical blow. She hesitated a moment, and if there had been any means of returning to the Pool farm, short of walking five miles in the snow, she would have taken it. Up the stairs and into the din. Evidently the auctioning of supper baskets was even now in progress. The roar of voices had broken out after the sale of a basket and now was subsiding under the earsplitting cracks of the auctioneer’s hammer. Through the crowded doorway Selina could catch a glimpse of him as he stood on a chair, the baskets piled before him. He used a barrel elevated on a box as his pulpit. The auctioneer was Adam Ooms who himself had once been the High Prairie school teacher. A fox-faced little man, bald, falsetto, the village clown with a solid foundation of shrewdness under his clowning and a tart layer of malice over it.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜小心保持着盒子的平衡，推开那扇通向木楼梯的门。厅堂在二楼。撞进她耳朵的那片喧嚣像结结实实挨了一记闷拳。她迟疑了一下——这会儿只要有一条回普尔农场的路，不致在雪地里步行五英里，她都会走。上楼，扎进喧嚣里。显然，晚饭篮子的竞卖这会儿正到热闹处。一只篮子卖出后轰起的人声刚落下去，又被拍卖师槌子震耳的爆响压下去。隔着挤挤挨挨的门洞，塞莉娜能瞥见他站在一把椅子上，面前堆着一只只篮子。他拿一只架在木箱上的桶当讲坛。拍卖师正是亚当·奥姆斯本人，从前他自己也当过高草原的教师。一个狐狸脸的小个子男人，秃顶，假嗓，村里的丑角——插科打诨底下垫着一层扎实的精明，上头又罩着一层发酸的刻薄。

===Original===
High and shrill came his voice. “What am I bid! What am I bid! Thirty cents! Thirty-five! Shame on you, gentlemen. What am I bid! Who’ll make it forty!”

===Chinese===
他的嗓音又高又尖。「给个价！给个价！三毛！三毛五！不害臊吗，先生们。给个价！谁出到四毛！」

===Original===
Selina felt a little thrill of excitement. She looked about for a place on which to lay her wraps. Every table, chair, hook, and rack in the hallway was piled with clothing. She espied a box that appeared empty, rolled her cloak, muffler, and hood into a neat bundle and, about to cast it into the box, saw, upturned to her from its depths, the round pink faces of the sleeping Kuyper twins, aged six months. From the big hall now came a great shouting, clapping of hands, stamping, catcalls. Another basket had been disposed of. Oh, dear! In desperation Selina placed her bundle on the floor in a corner, smoothed down the red cashmere, snatched up her lunch box and made for the doorway with the childish eagerness of one out of the crowd to be in it. She wondered where Maartje and Klaas Pool were in this close-packed roomful; and Roelf. In the doorway she found that broad black-coated backs shut off sight and ingress. She had written her name neatly on her lunch box. Now she was at a loss to find a way to reach Adam Ooms. She eyed the great-shouldered expanse just ahead of her. In desperation she decided to dig into it with a corner of her box. She dug, viciously. The back winced. Its owner turned. “Here! What﻿—!”

===Chinese===
塞莉娜感到一阵小小的兴奋震颤。她四下里找地方放外衣。门厅里每一张桌子、每一把椅子、每一只挂钩、每一副衣架都堆满了衣裳。她瞅见一只看着空着的箱子，把斗篷、围巾、风帽卷成齐整的一卷，正要扔将进去，却看见箱子深处仰面朝着她的，是凯珀家（Kuyper）双胞胎睡熟的两张粉红圆脸，才六个月大。大厅里这时爆出一阵大喊、拍手、跺脚、喝倒彩。又一只篮子出手了。哎呀天！塞莉娜没了法子，把衣卷往墙角地上一放，抚平红开司米，一把抓起午饭盒子，带着局外人巴不得挤进里头去的那股孩子气的急切，直奔门洞。她纳闷这么挤挤一堂的屋子里，玛尔切和克拉斯·普尔这会儿在哪儿；还有鲁尔夫。到了门洞她才发现，一堵堵宽阔的黑呢脊背把视线和进路全堵死了。她在自己午饭盒上端端正正写了名字。这会儿她可犯了难，寻不出一条道把盒子递到亚当·奥姆斯跟前。她打量着眼皮底下那一大片宽肩膀。情急之下，她决定拿盒子的一只角往里钻。她下狠劲地钻。那脊背一哆嗦。主人回过头来。「哎！干什——！」

===Original===
Selina looked up into the wrathful face of Pervus DeJong. Pervus DeJong looked down into the startled eyes of Selina Peake. Large enough eyes at any time; enormous now in her fright at what she had done.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜抬头，正对上珀维斯·德容那张愠怒的脸。珀维斯·德容低头，正对上塞莉娜·皮克那双惊惶的眼睛。本来就挺大的一双眼睛；这会儿叫她这一吓，瞪得老大。

===Original===
“I’m sorry! I’m﻿—sorry. I thought if I could﻿—there’s no way of getting my lunch box up there﻿—such a crowd﻿—”

===Chinese===
「对不起！对——不起。我想着要是能——我的午饭盒子没法递上去——人这么挤——」

===Original===
A slim, appealing, lovely little figure in the wine-red cashmere, amidst all those buxom bosoms, and overheated bodies, and flushed faces. His gaze left her reluctantly, settled on the lunch box, became, if possible, more bewildered. “That? Lunch box?”

===Chinese===
一个纤瘦、楚楚动人、可爱的小小人影，裹在酒红色开司米里，夹在满堂丰满的胸脯、燥热的身子、通红的脸膛中间。他的目光恋恋不舍地从她身上挪开，落到午饭盒上，若说还有可能，就更添了几分茫然。「这个？午饭盒？」

===Original===
“Yes. For the raffle. I’m the school teacher. Selina Peake.”

===Chinese===
「对。参加竞卖的。我是这儿教书的。塞莉娜·皮克。」

===Original===
He nodded. “I saw you in church Sunday.”

===Chinese===
他点点头。「礼拜天在教堂见过你。」

===Original===
“You did! I didn’t think you.﻿ ﻿… Did you?”

===Chinese===
「你见过！我还当你没瞧见呢。……真瞧见啦？」

===Original===
“Wait here. I’ll come back. Wait here.”

===Chinese===
「在这儿等着。我就回来。在这儿等着。」

===Original===
He took the shoe box. She waited.
