He ploughed his way through the crowd like a Juggernaut, reached Adam Ooms’s platform and placed the box inconspicuously next a colossal hamper that was one of a dozen grouped awaiting Adam’s attention. When he had made his way back to Selina he again said, “Wait,” and plunged down the wooden stairway. Selina waited. She had ceased to feel distressed at her inability to find the Pools in the crowd, a-tiptoe though she was. When presently he came back he had in his hand an empty wooden soapbox. This he upended in the doorway just behind the crowd stationed there. Selina mounted it; found her head a little above the level of his. She could survey the room from end to end. There were the Pools. She waved to Maartje; smiled at Roelf. He made as though to come toward her; did come part way, and was restrained by Maartje catching at his coat tail.

===Chinese===
他接过鞋盒。她等着。他像一辆贾格纳特神车（Juggernaut）从人堆里犁过去，挤到亚当·奥姆斯的台前，把盒子不显山不露水地挨着一只硕大无朋的提篮放下——那样的提篮一打一打排开，正候着亚当照应。他挤回塞莉娜身边，又说了声「等着」，便一头扎下木楼梯去。塞莉娜等着。她虽然还踮着脚尖张望，倒不再为寻不见普尔一家发慌了。不多时他回来了，手里拎着一只空的木头肥皂箱。他把箱子倒扣在门洞里人群的背后。塞莉娜踩上去；发觉自己的脑袋刚好比他高出一点点。整间屋子从这头到那头尽收眼底。普尔一家在那儿。她朝玛尔切挥手；朝鲁尔夫笑。他作势要朝她过来；也真走出了几步，叫玛尔切一把揪住衣角拦下了。

===Original===
Selina wished she could think of something to say. She looked down at Pervus DeJong. The back of his neck was pink, as though with effort. She thought, instinctively, “My goodness, he’s trying to think of something to say, too.” That, somehow, put her at her ease. She would wait until he spoke. His neck was now a deep red. The crowd surged back at some disturbance around Adam Ooms’s elevation. Selina teetered perilously on her box, put out a hand blindly, felt his great hard hand on her arm, steadying her.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜巴不得想出句什么话来说。她低头看珀维斯·德容。他的后颈泛着粉红，像是费劲费的。她本能地想：「我的天，他也在搜肠刮肚找话说呢。」不知怎么，这一来她倒自在多了。她就等他先开口。他的后颈这会儿红透了。人群叫亚当·奥姆斯那高台附近一阵骚乱挤得往后一涌。塞莉娜在箱子上危险地晃了晃，慌乱里伸出手去，摸到他的大手牢牢扣在她胳膊上，把她稳住了。

===Original===
“Quite a crowd, ain’t it?” The effort had reached its apex. The red of his neck began to recede.

===Chinese===
「人不少，是不？」那股子费劲总算到了顶。他后颈的红潮开始往回退。

===Original===
“Oh, quite!”

===Chinese===
「哦，不少！」

===Original===
“They ain’t all High Prairie. Some of ’em’s from Low Prairie way. New Haarlem, even.”

===Chinese===
「也不全是高草原的。有的是低草原（Low Prairie）那边的。连新哈勒姆的都有。」

===Original===
“Really!”

===Chinese===
「真的！」

===Original===
A pause. Another effort.

===Chinese===
冷场片刻。又一番挣扎。

===Original===
“How goes it school teaching?”

===Chinese===
「书教得怎么样？」

===Original===
“Oh﻿—it goes pretty well.”

===Chinese===
「哦——还凑合。」

===Original===
“You are little to be school teacher, anyway, ain’t you?”

===Chinese===
「话说，你这么点儿个子就当老师，是不是小了点儿？」

===Original===
“Little!” She drew herself up from her vantage point of the soapbox. “I’m bigger than you are.”

===Chinese===
「小！」她从肥皂箱这块居高临下的宝地上挺直了身子。「我可比你大。」

===Original===
They laughed at that as at an exquisite piece of repartee.

===Chinese===
两人为这一句笑起来，仿佛听见了妙到毫巅的一句机锋。

===Original===
Adam Oom’s gavel (a wooden potato masher) crashed for silence. “Ladies!” [Crash!] “And gents!” [Crash!] “Gents! Look what basket we’ve got here!”

===Chinese===
亚当·奥姆斯的法槌（一把木头土豆捣子）敲响要大家肃静。「女士们！」【砰！】「还有先生们！」【砰！】「先生们！瞧瞧咱们这儿是什么篮子！」

===Original===
Look indeed. A great hamper, grown so plethoric that it could no longer wear its cover. Its contents bellied into a mound smoothly covered with a fine white cloth whose glistening surface proclaimed it damask. A Himalaya among hampers. You knew that under that snowy crust lay gold that was fowl done crisply, succulently; emeralds in the form of gherkins; rubies that melted into strawberry preserves; cakes frosted like diamonds; to say nothing of such semiprecious jewels as potato salad; cheeses; sour cream to be spread on rye bread and butter; coffee cakes; crullers.

===Chinese===
可不是嘛，瞧吧。一只硕大无朋的提篮，塞得脑满肠肥，连盖子都合不拢了。内容物鼓成一座小山，上头平平整整苫一块细白桌布，布面亮泽莹然，自报家门：锦缎的货色。提篮中的喜马拉雅。你不用问也知道，那层雪白的地壳底下埋的是黄金——炸得酥脆多汁的禽肉；祖母液化身的小酸黄瓜；化作草莓酱的红宝石；糖霜莹亮如钻石的蛋糕；更不必说那些半宝石了：土豆沙拉，干酪，抹黄油黑麦面包的酸奶油，咖啡蛋糕，炸麻花。

===Original===
Crash! “The Widow Paarlenberg’s basket, ladies﻿—*and* gents! The Widow Paarlenberg! I don’t know what’s in it. You don’t know what’s in it. We don’t have to know what’s in it. Who has eaten Widow Paarlenberg’s chicken once don’t have to know. Who has eaten Widow Paarlenberg’s cake once don’t have to know. What am I bid on Widow Paarlenberg’s basket! What am I bid! WhatmIbidwhatmIbidwhatmIbid!” [Crash!]

===Chinese===
【砰！】「帕伦贝格寡妇的篮子，女士们——*还有*先生们！帕伦贝格寡妇的篮子！我不知道里头是什么。你们不知道里头是什么。咱们也不用知道里头是什么。吃过帕伦贝格寡妇的鸡的，不用知道。吃过帕伦贝格寡妇的蛋糕的，不用知道。帕伦贝格寡妇的篮子出多少！出多少！出多少出多少出多少出多少！」【砰！】

===Original===
The widow herself, very handsome in black silk, her gold neck chain rising and falling richly with the little flurry that now agitated her broad bosom, was seated in a chair against the wall not five feet from the auctioneer’s stand. She bridled now, blushed, cast down her eyes, cast up her eyes, succeeded in looking as unconscious as a complaisant Turkish slave girl on the block.

===Chinese===
寡妇本人一身黑绸，衣装鲜亮，那条金项链随着此刻搅动她宽阔胸脯的小小激动，富贵地一起一伏，坐在靠墙一把椅子里，离拍卖台不到五英尺。这时她扭捏起来，飞红了脸，眼睛往下一垂，又一翻，居然装出浑然不觉的模样，活像站上拍卖台、任人喊价的温顺土耳其女奴。

===Original===
Adam Ooms’s glance swept the hall. He leaned forward, his fox-like face fixed in a smile. From the widow herself, seated so prominently at his right, his gaze marked the young blades of the village; the old bucks; youths and widowers and bachelors. Here was the prize of the evening. Around, in a semicircle, went his keen glance until it reached the tall figure towering in the doorway﻿—reached it, and rested there. His gimlet eyes seemed to bore their way into Pervus DeJong’s steady stare. He raised his right arm aloft, brandishing the potato masher. The whole room fixed its gaze on the blond head in the doorway. “Speak up! Young men of High Prairie! Heh, you, Pervus DeJong! WhatmIbidwhatmIbidwhatmIbid!”

===Chinese===
亚当·奥姆斯的目光扫过大厅。他探身向前，那张狐狸脸凝着一个笑。从端坐在他右手边、位置显眼的寡妇本人起，他的目光一路点过村里的翩翩少年郎；老当益壮的壮汉；小伙儿、鳏夫与光棍们。今晚的头彩在这儿了。他敏锐的目光扫过去，扫成半个圆圈，一直扫到门洞里那个高出众人一头的身影——扫到，便钉住不动了。他那双锥子眼像要钻透珀维斯·德容沉静的注视。他右臂高高举起，挥舞着土豆捣子。全屋子的目光都钉在门洞里那颗金发的脑袋上。「喊价呀！高草原的年轻人！嘿，说你呢，珀维斯·德容！出多少出多少出多少！」

===Original===
“Fifty cents!” The bid came from Gerrit Pon at the other end of the hall. A dashing offer, as a start, in this district where one dollar often represented the profits on a whole load of market truck brought to the city.

===Chinese===
「五毛！」报价来自大厅那一头的格里特·庞。在这个一块钱往往就是一整车鲜菜进城的全部利润的地方，起价出到这个数，算得上一掷了。

===Original===
Crash! went the potato masher. “Fifty cents I’m bid. Who’ll make it seventy-five? Who’ll make it seventy-five?”

===Chinese===
【砰！】土豆捣子落下。「五毛。谁出七毛五？谁出七毛五？」

===Original===
“Sixty!” Johannes Ambuul, a widower, his age more than the sum of his bid.

===Chinese===
「六毛！」约翰内斯·安布尔（Johannes Ambuul），一个鳏夫，岁数比他出的价码还大。

===Original===
“Seventy!” Gerrit Pon.

===Chinese===
「七毛！」格里特·庞。

===Original===
Adam Ooms whispered it﻿—hissed it. “S-s-s-seventy. Ladies and gents, I wouldn’t repeat out loud sucha figger. I would be ashamed. Look at this basket, gents, and then you can say﻿ ﻿… s-s-seventy!”

===Chinese===
亚当·奥姆斯压着嗓子说——嘶嘶地从牙缝里挤。「七——七——七——毛。女士们先生们，这么个数我都羞于大声说出口。说了丢人。瞧瞧这只篮子，先生们，瞧完你们还好意思……七——七——七毛！」

===Original===
“Seventy-five!” the cautious Ambuul.

===Chinese===
「七毛五！」小心谨慎的安布尔。

===Original===
Scarlet, flooding her face, belied the widow’s outward air of composure. Pervus DeJong, standing beside Selina, viewed the proceedings with an air of detachment. High Prairie was looking at him expectantly, openly. The widow bit her red lip, tossed her head. Pervus DeJong returned the auctioneer’s meaning smirk with the mild gaze of a disinterested outsider. High Prairie, Low Prairie, and New Haarlem sat tense, like an audience at a play. Here, indeed, was drama being enacted in a community whose thrills were all too rare.

===Chinese===
一片猩红涨满两颊，戳穿了寡妇表面上的镇定。珀维斯·德容站在塞莉娜身旁，带着置身事外的神气看这场热闹。高草原的人都眼巴巴、直勾勾地望着他。寡妇咬着她那红嘴唇，把头一甩。拍卖师抛去一个别有会心的一笑，珀维斯·德容回敬的，是一副事不关己的看客温温和和的目光。高草原、低草原、新哈勒姆一齐屏住气，像戏园子里的看客。可不是嘛，在这个刺激少得可怜的乡社里，一出活戏正当面开演。

===Original===
“Gents!” Adam Ooms’s voice took on a tearful note﻿—the tone of one who is more hurt than angry. “Gents!” Slowly, with infinite reverence, he lifted one corner of the damask cloth that concealed the hamper’s contents﻿—lifted it and peered within as at a treasure. At what he saw there he started back dramatically, at once rapturous, despairing, amazed. He rolled his eyes. He smacked his lips. He rubbed his stomach. The sort of dumb show that, since the days of the Greek drama, has been used to denote gastronomic delight.

===Chinese===
「先生们！」亚当·奥姆斯的嗓音带上了一股哭腔——受了伤多过动了气的调门。「先生们！」他慢条斯理、无限恭敬地，掀起苫着篮子内容的那一角锦缎桌布——掀起来朝里张望，像窥一件宝物。看见里头的东西，他戏剧性地一个倒仰，登时如痴如狂、如丧考妣、惊叹不置。他翻白眼。他咂嘴。他揉肚子。这一套哑剧，打希腊戏剧的年代起，就专门用来表示口腹大悦。

===Original===
“Eighty!” was wrenched suddenly from Goris Von Vuuren, the nineteen-year-old fat and gluttonous son of a prosperous New Haarlem farmer.

===Chinese===
「八毛！」这一声是突然从戈里斯·范武伦（Goris Von Vuuren）身上拧出来的，新哈勒姆一户殷实农家十九岁的胖儿子，一个馋痨胚。

===Original===
Adam Ooms rubbed brisk palms together. “Now then! A dollar! A dollar! It’s an insult to this basket to make it less than a dollar.” He lifted the cover again, sniffed, appeared overcome. “Gents, if it wasn’t for Mrs. Ooms sitting there I’d make it a dollar myself and a bargain. A dollar! Am I bid a dollar!” He leaned far forward over his improvised pulpit. “Did I hear you say a dollar, Pervus DeJong?” DeJong stared, immovable, unabashed. His very indifference was contagious. The widow’s bountiful basket seemed to shrink before one’s eyes. “Eighty-eighty-eighty-eighty﻿—gents! I’m going to tell you something. I’m going to whisper a secret.” His lean face was veined with craftiness. “Gents. Listen. It isn’t chicken in this beautiful basket. It isn’t chicken. It’s”﻿—a dramatic pause﻿—“it’s *roast duck*!” He swayed back, mopped his brow with his red handkerchief, held one hand high in the air. His last card.

===Chinese===
亚当·奥姆斯急急搓着双掌。「这就对了！一块！一块！这篮子卖不上块钱，是这篮子的耻辱。」他又掀开盖子闻了闻，一副魂飞天外的样子。「先生们，要不是奥姆斯太太坐在那儿，我自己就出一块了，还是捡便宜。一块！有人出一块吗！」他在他那临时讲坛上远远探出身去。「我听见你说一块了吗，珀维斯·德容？」德容直挺挺瞪着，纹丝不动，面无愧色。他这份淡漠简直有传染性。寡妇那只丰饶的篮子在人眼里仿佛都缩小了一圈。「八毛——八毛——八毛——八毛——先生们！我要告诉你们一件事。我要凑耳朵说个秘密。」他那张瘦脸上爬满了狡狯的纹路。「先生们。听着。这只漂亮篮子里装的不是鸡。不是鸡。是」——戏剧性的一顿——「是*烤鸭*！」他往后一仰，掏出红手帕抹了抹脑门，一只手高高举在空中。他的最后一张牌。

===Original===
“Eighty-five!” groaned the fat Goris Von Vuuren.

===Chinese===
「八毛五！」胖子戈里斯·范武伦哀号一声。

===Original===
“Eighty-five! Eighty-five! Eightyfiveeightyfiveeightyfive eighty-five! Gents! Gen-tle-men! Eighty-five once! Eighty-five﻿—twice!” [Crash!] “Gone to Goris Von Vuuren for eighty-five.”

===Chinese===
「八毛五！八毛五！八毛五八毛五八毛五八毛五！先生们！先——生——们！八毛五一次！八毛五——两次！」【砰！】「戈里斯·范武伦，八毛五成交。」

===Original===
A sigh went up from the assemblage; a sigh that was the wind before the storm. There followed a tornado of talk. It crackled and thundered. The rich Widow Paarlenberg would have to eat her supper with Von Vuuren’s boy, the great thick Goris. And there in the doorway, talking to teacher as if they had known each other for years, was Pervus DeJong with his money in his pocket. It was as good as a play.

===Chinese===
全场升起一声长叹；那是暴风雨前头的一阵风。跟着来了一场谈话的龙卷风。噼里啪啦，电闪雷鸣。有钱的帕伦贝格寡妇，这回竟得跟范武伦家的小子、那个傻大黑粗的戈里斯一块儿吃晚饭了。而门洞里，正跟女教师说话、说得像相识了多年的老朋友似的，正是珀维斯·德容，钱还在他口袋里揣着。这比看戏还过瘾。

===Original===
Adam Ooms was angry. His lean, fox-like face became pinched with spite. He prided himself on his antics as auctioneer; and his chef d’œuvre had brought a meagre eighty-five cents, besides doubtless winning him the enmity of that profitable store customer, the Widow Paarlenberg. Goris Von Vuuren came forward to claim his prize amidst shouting, clapping, laughter. The great hamper was handed down to him; an ample, rich-looking burden, its handle folded comfortably over its round stomach, its white cover so glistening with starch and ironing that it gave back the light from the big lamp above the auctioneer’s stand. As Goris Von Vuuren lifted it his great shoulders actually sagged. Its contents promised satiety even to such a feeder as he. A grin, half sheepish, half triumphant, creased his plump pink face.

===Chinese===
亚当·奥姆斯恼了。他那张瘦狐狸脸气得缩紧，一股子毒劲。他以拍卖师的花招自命不凡；而他这压卷之作只换来可怜巴巴的八毛五，此外八成还赢了帕伦贝格寡妇的仇——铺子里一位有钱的主顾。戈里斯·范武伦在呼喊、掌声、哄笑里上前认领他的奖品。那只大提篮递到他手里；好一份富态扎眼的担子，提梁舒舒服服搭在它滚圆的肚子上，白盖布浆烫得雪亮，把拍卖台上头那盏大灯的光都反照回去。戈里斯·范武伦一提，那副宽肩膀竟往下一坠。这里头的东西，管保他这样的大肚汉也能吃个膘满肠肥。一个咧到耳根的笑，一半腼腆一半得胜，挤上他那张胖粉脸。

===Original===
Adam Ooms scuffled about among the many baskets at his feet. His nostrils looked pinched and his skinny hands shook a little as he searched for one small object.

===Chinese===
亚当·奥姆斯在他脚边那一大堆篮子里乱翻。他寻摸那件小东西的时候，鼻翼捏得紧紧的，一双瘦手微微发抖。

===Original===
When he stood upright once more he was smiling. His little eyes gleamed. His wooden sceptre pounded for silence. High in one hand, balanced daintily on his finger tips, he held Selina’s little white shoe box, with its red ribbon binding it, and the plume of evergreen stuck in the ribbon. Affecting great solicitude he brought it down then to read the name written on it; held it aloft again, smirking.

===Chinese===
再站直身来时，他脸上已经堆起笑。小眼睛放光。他的木头权杖敲出肃静。一只手高高举起，指尖上轻巧地拈着塞莉娜那只小小的白鞋盒，红缎带扎着，缎带里插着那枝常青。他装出十二分的小心郑重，把盒子放低下来，读上面写的名字；又高高举起，嘻嘻地笑。

===Original===
He said nothing. Grinning, he held it high. He turned his body at the waist from side to side, so that all might see. The eyes of those before him still held a mental picture of the huge hamper, food-packed, that had just been handed down. The contrast was too absurd, too cruel. A ripple of laughter swept the room; rose; swelled to a roar. Adam Ooms drew his mouth down solemnly. His little finger elegantly crooked, he pendulumed the box to right and left. He swerved his beady eyes from side to side. He waited with a nice sense of the dramatic until the laughter had reached its height, then held up a hand for silence. A great scraping “Ahem!” as he cleared his throat threatened to send the crowd off again.

===Chinese===
他一言不发。咧着嘴，高高举着。他腰部以上左右拧转，好让人人看个明白。他跟前那些人的眼睛里，还留着刚递下去的那只塞满吃食的大提篮的影子。这对比太荒唐，也太刻毒。一层笑意的涟漪扫过全场；涨起来；汇成哄堂大笑。亚当·奥姆斯把嘴往下撇，煞有介事。小指头优雅地翘着，他把盒子像钟摆似的左右荡。他的小圆眼滴溜溜左右乱转。他带着精湛的戏剧分寸感，直等到笑声涨到顶，才举手示意肃静。他清嗓子时那一声刺耳的「啊咳」，险些又把全场送进新一轮狂笑。

===Original===
“Ladies﻿—*and* gents! Here’s a dainty little tidbit. Here’s something not only for the inner man, but a feast for the eye. Well, boys, if the last lot was too much for you this lot ought to be just about right. If the food ain’t quite enough for you, you can tie the ribbon in the lady’s hair and put the posy in your buttonhole and there you are. *There* you are! What’s more, the lady herself goes with it. You don’t get a country girl with this here box, gents. A city girl, you can tell by looking at it, just. And who is she? Who did up this dainty little box just big enough for two?” He inspected it again, solemnly, and added, as an afterthought, “If you ain’t feeling specially hungry. Who?﻿—” He looked about, apishly.

===Chinese===
「女士们——*还有*先生们！这儿是一件精致的小点心。这道菜不光喂得饱肚皮，还是眼睛的一场盛宴。怎么样，小伙子们，上一件你们消受不起，这一件保管正合胃口。吃的不够，你们还可以把缎带系上小姐的头发，把小花插进扣眼，这就齐了。*这*不就齐了！再说，小姐本人也随盒子奉送。先生们，这盒子里装的可不是乡下丫头。是位城里小姐，一望便知，可不是。她是谁呢？谁打点的这只精致小盒，装两个人的刚刚好？」他又煞有介事地端详一番，又补上一句，像是临时想起，「要是您不算特别饿的话。是谁？——」他猴儿似的四下张望。

===Original===
Selina’s cheeks matched her gown. Her eyes were wide and dark with the effort she was making to force back the hot haze threatening them. Why had she mounted this wretched soapbox! Why had she come to this hideous party! Why had she come to High Prairie! Why!﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
塞莉娜的两颊红得跟她的裙子一个颜色。她拼命把涌上来的热雾往回逼，眼睛睁得又大又黑。她干吗要踩上这只倒霉的肥皂箱！她干吗要来这场要命的聚会！她干吗要来高草原！干吗！……

===Original===
“Miss Selina Peake, that’s who. Miss Se-li-na Peake!”

===Chinese===
「正是塞莉娜·皮克小姐。塞——莉——娜·皮克小姐！」

===Original===
A hundred balloon faces pulled by a single cord turned toward her as she stood there on the box for all to see. They swam toward her. She put up a hand to push them back.

===Chinese===
一百张气球脸像拴在一根绳上，齐刷刷转向她，她站在箱子上，供人观瞻。那些脸朝她涌过来。她抬起一只手去推。

===Original===
“What’m I bid! What’m I bid! What’m I bid for this here lovely little toothful, gents! Start her up!”

===Chinese===
「出多少！出多少！这么可爱的一小口出多少，先生们！开个头！」

===Original===
“Five cents!” piped up old Johannes Ambuul, with a snicker. The tittering crowd broke into a guffaw. Selina was conscious of a little sick feeling at the pit of her stomach. Through the haze she saw the widow’s face, no longer sulky, but smiling now. She saw Roelf’s dear dark head. His face was set, like a man’s. He was coming toward her, or trying to, but the crowd wedged him in, small as he was among those great bodies. She lost sight of him. How hot it was! how hot﻿ ﻿… An arm at her waist. Some one had mounted the little box and stood teetering there beside her, pressed against her slightly, reassuringly. Pervus DeJong. Her head was on a level with his great shoulder now. They stood together in the doorway, on the soapbox, for all High Prairie to see.

===Chinese===
「五分钱！」老约翰内斯·安布尔尖着嗓子起哄，带一声窃笑。嗤嗤窃笑的人群轰然大笑。塞莉娜只觉得胃底下一阵小小的发恶心。透过那层热雾她看见寡妇的脸，不再赌气，这会儿倒笑盈盈的了。她看见鲁尔夫那颗可爱的黑发脑袋。他绷着脸，像条汉子。他正朝她挤过来，或者想挤过来，可他在那些大身板当中太瘦小，人潮把他楔住了。她看不见他了。多热呀！多热……一条胳膊揽住她的腰。有人登上了那只小箱子，摇摇晃晃立在她身旁，轻轻地、稳稳地贴着她。珀维斯·德容。她的头这会儿正齐着他那伟岸的肩膀。两人并肩站在门洞的肥皂箱上，任全高草原观看。

===Original===
“Five cents I’m bid for this lovely little mouthful put up by the school teacher’s own fair hands. Five cents! Five﻿—”

===Chinese===
「五分钱，买女教师一双素手打点的这一小口。五分钱！五——」

===Original===
“One dollar!” Pervus DeJong.

===Chinese===
「一块！」珀维斯·德容。

===Original===
The balloon faces were suddenly punctured with holes. High Prairie’s jaw dropped with astonishment. Its mouth stood open.

===Chinese===
那一百张气球脸登时叫戳了窟窿。高草原惊得下巴都掉了。嘴张着合不上。

===Original===
There was nothing plain about Selina now. Her dark head was held high, and his fair one beside it made a vivid foil. The purchase of the wine-coloured cashmere was at last justified.

===Chinese===
这会儿塞莉娜可一点也不平庸了。她的黑发头颅高高扬起，旁边他那一颗金发的，正添一道鲜亮的衬色。那件酒红色开司米，到今天总算买得值了。

===Original===
“And ten!” cackled old Johannes Ambuul, his rheumy eyes on Selina.

===Chinese===
「加一毛！」老约翰内斯·安布尔咯咯地喊，昏花的眼睛黏在塞莉娜身上。

===Original===
Art and human spitefulness struggled visibly for mastery in Adam Ooms’s face﻿—and art won. The auctioneer triumphed over the man. The term “crowd psychology” was unknown to him, but he was artist enough to sense that some curious magic process, working through this roomful of people, had transformed the little white box, from a thing despised and ridiculed, into an object of beauty, of value, of infinite desirability. He now eyed it in a catalepsy of admiration.

===Chinese===
艺术和人的促狭在亚当·奥姆斯脸上明明白白争夺高下——艺术赢了。拍卖师战胜了那个人。「群体心理」这个词他没听说过，可他天生是位艺术家，感觉得出来：这满屋子的人身上正起着什么奇妙的魔术，把那只小盒子从一件受尽讪笑的东西，变成了一件美的东西、值钱的东西、人人梦寐以求的东西。这会儿他看它的眼神，膜拜得都僵住了。

===Original===
“One-ten I’m bid for this box all tied with a ribbon to match the gown of the girl who brought it. Gents, you get the ribbon, the lunch, *and* the girl. And only one-ten bid for all that. Gents! Gents! Remember, it ain’t only a lunch﻿—it’s a picture. It pleases the eye. Do I hear one﻿—”

===Chinese===
「一块一，买这只红缎带扎着的盒子，缎带还正配送它来的姑娘一身衣裳。先生们，你们到手的是缎带、晚饭，*外加*姑娘本人。才一块一。先生们！先生们！记住，这不只是一顿饭——这是一幅画。养眼！我听见谁出一——」

===Original===
“Five bits!” Barend DeRoo, of Low Prairie, in the lists. A strapping young Dutchman, the Brom Bones of the district. Aaltje Huff, in a fit of pique at his indifference, had married to spite him. Cornelia Vinke, belle of New Haarlem, was said to be languishing for love of him. He drove to the Haymarket with his load of produce and played cards all night on the wagon under the gas torches while the street girls of the neighbourhood assailed him in vain. Six feet three, his red face shone now like a harvest moon above the crowd. A merry, mischievous eye that laughed at Pervus DeJong and his dollar bid.

===Chinese===
「一块六毛二分五！」巴伦德·德鲁（Barend DeRoo），低草原来的，入场应战。一条魁伟的荷兰青年汉子，本乡本土的布罗姆·博恩斯（Brom Bones）。阿尔特耶·赫夫（Aaltje Huff）赌气他无情，负气嫁了人来泄愤。新哈勒姆的美人儿科妮莉亚·芬克（Cornelia Vinke），据说正为他害相思。他赶上满车青菜去海马克特，整夜在瓦斯火把底下坐在马车上打牌，左近的站街姑娘们怎么撩也撩他不动。身高六英尺三，这会儿他那张红脸像一轮丰收的月亮，高高悬在人堆上头。一双快活促狭的眼睛，正笑话着珀维斯·德容和他那一块钱。

===Original===
“Dollar and a half!” A high clear voice﻿—a boy’s voice. Roelf.

===Chinese===
「一块五！」一个又高又清的嗓音——男孩子的嗓音。鲁尔夫。

===Original===
“Oh, no!” said Selina aloud. But she was unheard in the gabble. Roelf had once confided to her that he had saved three dollars and fifty cents in the last three years. Five dollars would purchase a set of tools that his mind had been fixed on for months past. Selina saw Klaas Pool’s look of astonishment changing to anger. Saw Maartje Pool’s quick hand on his arm, restraining him.

===Chinese===
「哦，别！」塞莉娜说出声来。可她的声音淹没在嘈杂里。鲁尔夫有一次向她交过底：这三年里他攒下了三块五毛钱。五块钱能置下一整套工具，他惦记那套工具已经好几个月了。塞莉娜看见克拉斯·普尔脸上的惊异变了恼怒。看见玛尔切·普尔的手飞快按上他的胳膊，把他拦住了。

===Original===
“Two dollars!” Pervus DeJong.

===Chinese===
「两块！」珀维斯·德容。

===Original===
“Twotwotwotwotwotwo!” Adam Ooms in a frenzy of salesmanship.

===Chinese===
「两块两块两块两块两两两！」亚当·奥姆斯卖兴大发。

===Original===
“And ten.” Johannes Ambuul’s cautious bid.

===Chinese===
「加一毛。」约翰内斯·安布尔谨慎的一注。

===Original===
“Two and a quarter.” Barend DeRoo.

===Chinese===
「两块两毛五。」巴伦德·德鲁。

===Original===
“Two-fifty!” Pervus DeJong.

===Chinese===
「两块五！」珀维斯·德容。

===Original===
“Three dollars!” The high voice of the boy. It cracked a little on the last syllable, and the crowd laughed.

===Chinese===
「三块！」那男孩的高嗓门。末一个音节劈了一下，人群笑了。

===Original===
“Three-three-three-three-threethreethree. Three once﻿—”

===Chinese===
「三块三块三块三块三三三。三块一次——」

===Original===
“And a half.” Pervus DeJong.

===Chinese===
「加五毛。」珀维斯·德容。

===Original===
“Three sixty.”

===Chinese===
「三块六。」

===Original===
“Four!” DeRoo.

===Chinese===
「四块！」德鲁。

===Original===
“And ten.”

===Chinese===
「加一毛。」

===Original===
The boy’s voice was heard no more.

===Chinese===
男孩子的嗓音再听不见了。

===Original===
“I wish they’d stop,” whispered Selina.

===Chinese===
「他们别再抬了才好，」塞莉娜低声说。

===Original===
“Five!” Pervus DeJong.

===Chinese===
「五块！」珀维斯·德容。

===Original===
“Six!” DeRoo, his face very red.

===Chinese===
「六块！」德鲁，脸红透了。

===Original===
“And ten.”

===Chinese===
「加一毛。」

===Original===
“Seven!”

===Chinese===
「七块！」

===Original===
“It’s only jelly sandwiches,” said Selina to DeJong, in a panic.

===Chinese===
「不过是果酱三明治呀，」塞莉娜慌了神，对德容说。

===Original===
“Eight!” Johannes Ambuul, gone mad.

===Chinese===
「八块！」约翰内斯·安布尔，疯魔了。

===Original===
“Nine!” DeRoo.

===Chinese===
「九块！」德鲁。

===Original===
“Nine! Nine I’m bid! Nine-nine-nine! Who’ll make it﻿—”

===Chinese===
「九块！九块！九块九块九块！谁再出——」

===Original===
“Let him have it. The cup cakes fell a little. Don’t﻿—”

===Chinese===
「让给他吧。小蛋糕有点塌了。别——」

===Original===
“Ten!” said Pervus DeJong.

===Chinese===
「十块！」珀维斯·德容说。

===Original===
Barend DeRoo shrugged his great shoulders.

===Chinese===
巴伦德·德鲁耸了耸他那副大肩膀。

===Original===
“Ten-ten-ten. Do I hear eleven? Do I hear ten-fifty! Ten-ten-ten tententententententen! Gents! Ten once. Ten twice! Gone!﻿—for ten dollars to Pervus DeJong. And a bargain.” Adam Ooms mopped his bald head and his cheeks and the damp spot under his chin.

===Chinese===
「十块十块十块。有出十一的吗？有出十块五的吗！十块十块十块十十十十十十十！先生们！十块一次。十块两次！成交！——十块钱，珀维斯·德容。捡了个大便宜。」亚当·奥姆斯抹了抹他的秃脑门、两颊，还有下巴颏底下那块湿。

===Original===
Ten dollars. Adam Ooms knew, as did all the countryside, this was not the sum of ten dollars merely. No basket of food, though it contained nightingales’ tongues, the golden apple of Atalanta, wines of rare vintage, could have been adequate recompense for these ten dollars. They represented sweat and blood; toil and hardship; hours under the burning prairie sun at midday; work doggedly carried on through the drenching showers of spring; nights of restless sleep snatched an hour at a time under the sky in the Chicago market place; miles of weary travel down the rude corduroy road between High Prairie and Chicago, now up to the hubs in mud, now blinded by dust and blowing sand.

===Chinese===
十块钱。亚当·奥姆斯知道，全乡的人也都知道，这可不是区区十块钱的数目。任凭一只吃食篮子里装的是夜莺舌、阿塔兰忒（Atalanta）的金苹果、陈年名酿，也抵不过这十块钱。这十块钱是汗，是血；是辛劳，是苦熬；是正午草原烈日底下熬的钟点；是春天瓢泼大雨里硬着头皮死扛的活计；是芝加哥市场露天下一个钟头一个钟头抓来眯一觉的惊觉的夜；是高草原到芝加哥之间那条坎坷圆木路上一英里一英里的疲惫跋涉——时而车轮陷泥齐毂，时而叫尘土飞沙迷了眼。

===Original===
A sale at Christie’s, with a miniature going for a million, could not have met with a deeper hush, a more dramatic babble following the hush.

===Chinese===
佳士得（Christie’s）拍卖行里一幅微型画像拍出一百万，也不见得能博得比这更深的鸦雀无声，和肃静之后比这更富戏剧性的一阵嗡嗡。

===Original===
They ate their lunch together in one corner of Adam Ooms’s hall. Selina opened the box and took out the devilled eggs, and the cup cakes that had fallen a little, and the apples, and the sandwiches sliced very, very thin. The coldly appraising eye of all High Prairie, Low Prairie, and New Haarlem watched this sparse provender emerge from the ribbon-tied shoe box. She offered him a sandwich. It looked infinitesimal in his great paw. Suddenly all Selina’s agony of embarrassment was swept away, and she was laughing, not wildly or hysterically, but joyously and girlishly. She sank her little white teeth into one of the absurd sandwiches and looked at him, expecting to find him laughing, too. But he wasn’t laughing. He looked very earnest, and his blue eyes were fixed hard on the bit of bread in his hand, and his face was very red and clean-shaven. He bit into the sandwich and chewed it solemnly. And Selina thought: “Why, the dear thing! The great big dear thing! And he might have been eating breast of duck﻿ ﻿… Ten dollars!” Aloud she said, “What made you do it?”

===Chinese===
两人就在亚当·奥姆斯厅堂的一角共进了晚饭。塞莉娜打开盒子，取出魔鬼蛋，取出微微塌陷的小蛋糕，取出苹果，取出切得极薄极薄的三明治。高草原、低草原和新哈勒姆全体人马冷眼估价的目光，瞧着这点寒素吃食从红缎带扎着的鞋盒里一样样现身。她递给他一块三明治。搁在他那只大手掌里，小得没了看头。忽然间，塞莉娜那一腔窘迫一扫而空，她笑起来，不是狂笑，也不是歇斯底里，而是欢欢喜喜、女孩子气的笑。她那口雪白的小牙咬进一块荒唐的小三明治，抬眼看他，满心指望发现他也在笑。可他没有笑。他神色郑重，蓝眼睛直勾勾钉着手里的那一小片面包，脸膛红红的，刮得干干净净。他咬了一口三明治，郑重其事地嚼着。塞莉娜心想：「哎呀，这可爱的人！这高大可爱的人！他满可以吃鸭胸肉的……十块钱啊！」嘴上却说：「你干吗要这样？」

===Original===
“I don’t know. I don’t know.” Then, “You looked so little. And they were making fun. Laughing.” He looked very earnest, and his blue eyes were fixed hard on the sandwich, and his face was very red.

===Chinese===
「不知道。我就是不知道。」顿了顿，又说：「你看上去太小了。他们还取笑。笑。」他神色郑重，蓝眼睛直勾勾钉着那块三明治，脸膛红红的。

===Original===
“That’s a very foolish reason for throwing away ten dollars,” Selina said, severely.

===Chinese===
「为这个扔掉十块钱，理由可够蠢的，」塞莉娜正色道。

===Original===
He seemed not to hear her; bit ruminantly into one of the cup cakes. Suddenly: “I can’t hardly write at all, only to sign my name and like that.”

===Chinese===
他像没听见她的话；倒嚼似的咬了一口小蛋糕。冷不丁地：「我几乎不会写字，只会签个自己的名，再就是那一类的一两笔。」

===Original===
“Read?”

===Chinese===
「念书呢？」

===Original===
“Only to spell out the words. Anyways I don’t get time for reading. But figuring I wish I knew. ’Rithmetic. I can figger some, but those fellows in Haymarket they are too sharp for me. They do numbers in their head﻿—like that, so quick.”

===Chinese===
「也就一个字一个字往外拼。再说我也腾不出念书的工夫。可算术我巴不得会。算学。我也能算两下子，可海马克特那些家伙，精得我斗不过。人家脑子里就算出来了——啪，这么快。」

===Original===
Selina leaned toward him. “I’ll teach you. I’ll teach you.”

===Chinese===
塞莉娜朝他倾过身去。「我教你。我教你。」

===Original===
“How do you mean, teach me?”

===Chinese===
「怎么讲，教我？」

===Original===
“Evenings.”

===Chinese===
「晚上。」

===Original===
He looked down at his great calloused palms, then up at her. “What would you take for pay?”

===Chinese===
他低头看看自己那双满是老茧的大手心，又抬头看她。「工钱你要多少？」

===Original===
“Pay! I don’t want any pay.” She was genuinely shocked.

===Chinese===
「工钱！我不要什么工钱。」她是真吓了一跳。

===Original===
His face lighted up with a sudden thought. “Tell you what. My place is just this side the school, next to Bouts’s place. I could start for you the fire, mornings, in the school. And thaw the pump and bring in a pail of water. This month, and January and February and part of March, even, now I don’t go to market on account it’s winter, I could start you the fire. Till spring. And I could come maybe three times a week, evenings, to Pool’s place, for lessons.” He looked so helpless, so humble, so huge; and the more pathetic for his hugeness.

===Chinese===
他脸上忽地一亮，来了个主意。「这么着吧。我那地方就在学校这一边，挨着鲍茨（Bouts）家。早上我可以上学校替你生炉子。再把水泵烤化，提一桶水进来。这个月，一月、二月，连三月的一截——反正是冬天，我不上市——我都能替你生炉子。生到开春。晚上呢，一礼拜兴许能来三回，上普尔家上课。」他那副样子那么无措，那么谦卑，那么大块头；块头越大，越叫人心疼。

===Original===
She felt a little rush of warmth toward him that was at once impersonal and maternal. She thought again, “Why, the dear thing! The great helpless big thing! How serious he is! And funny.” He was indeed both serious and funny, with the ridiculous cup cake in his great hand, his eyes wide and ruminant, his face ruddier than ever, his forehead knotted with earnestness. She laughed, suddenly, a gay little laugh, and he, after a puzzled pause, joined her companionably.

===Chinese===
她心里对他涌起一小股暖流，既不关男女之情，又近乎母性。她又想：「哎呀，这可爱的人！这孤助无援的大东西！他多认真哪！还有多好笑。」他确实又认真又好笑，那只大手里捏着块荒唐的小蛋糕，眼睛睁得圆圆的、出着神，脸膛红得赛过往常，脑门上拧起一股子诚心的疙瘩。她忽然笑了，脆生生的一小声，他怔了一怔，也随和地跟着笑起来。

===Original===
“Three evenings a week,” repeated Selina, then, from the depths of her ignorance. “Why, I’d love to. I’d﻿—love to.”

===Chinese===
「一礼拜三个晚上，」塞莉娜重复道，随即，从她那无知的深渊底上：「哎呀，我愿意极了。我——愿意极了。」

## VI / 第六章

===Original===
The evenings turned out to be Tuesdays, Thursdays, and Saturdays. Supper was over by six-thirty in the Pool household. Pervus was there by seven, very clean as to shirt, his hair brushed till it shone; shy, and given to dropping his hat and bumping against chairs, and looking solemn. Selina was torn between pity and mirth. If only he had blustered. A blustering big man puts the world on the defensive. A gentle giant disarms it.

===Chinese===
上课的晚上定下来是礼拜二、礼拜四、礼拜六。普尔家的晚饭六点半就吃完了。七点刚过珀维斯便到，衬衫洗得干干净净，头发梳得油光锃亮；人却腼腆，不是掉了帽子，就是撞上椅子，一张脸绷得端端正正。塞莉娜又怜又想笑，两头受着。他要是个咋咋呼呼的主儿倒好了。大呼小叫的大个子，叫全世界摆开守势；温厚的巨人，却叫全世界卸下武装。

===Original===
Selina got out her McBride’s *Grammar* and Duffy’s *Arithmetic*, and together they started to parse verbs, paper walls, dig cisterns, and extract square roots. They found study impossible at the oilcloth-covered kitchen table, with the Pool household eddying about it. Jakob built a fire in the parlour stove and there they sat, teacher and pupil, their feet resting cosily on the gleaming nickel railing that encircled the wood burner.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜翻出她的麦克布莱德《语法》（McBride’s *Grammar*）和达菲《算术》（Duffy’s *Arithmetic*），两个人便一道解析起动词来，裱糊起墙壁来，挖掘起水窖来，还开起平方根来。铺油布的厨房桌子上是用不了功的——普尔全家围着它打旋儿。雅各布在客厅炉子里生了火，师生二人便坐在那儿，脚舒舒服服地搭在柴炉四周那道锃亮的镍栏杆上。

===Original===
On the evening of the first lesson Roelf had glowered throughout supper and had disappeared into the work-shed, whence issued a great sound of hammering, sawing, and general clatter. He and Selina had got into the way of spending much time together, in or out of doors. They skated on Vander Sijde’s pond; together with the shrieking pigtails they coasted on the little slope that led down from Kuyper’s woods to the main road, using sleds that had been put together by Roelf. On bad days they read or studied. Not Sundays merely, but many weekday evenings were spent thus. Selina was determined that Roelf should break away from the uncouth speech of the countryside; that he should at least share with her the somewhat sketchy knowledge gained at Miss Fister’s select school. She, the woman of almost twenty, never talked down to this boy of twelve. The boy worshipped her inarticulately. She had early discovered that he had a feeling for beauty﻿—beauty of line, texture, colour, and grouping﻿—that was rare in one of his years. The feel of a satin ribbon in his fingers; the orange and rose of a sunset; the folds of the wine-red cashmere dress; the cadence of a spoken line, brought a look to his face that startled her. She had a battered volume of Tennyson. When first she read him the line beginning, “Elaine the fair, Elaine the lovable, Elaine, the lily maid of Astolat﻿—” he had uttered a little exclamation. She, glancing up from her book, had found his eyes wide, bright, and luminous in his lean dark face.

===Chinese===
头一回上课那天晚上，鲁尔夫整个晚饭都沉着脸，一撂饭碗就钻进作坊没了影，里头传出一大片锤打、锯割、乒乒乓乓的动静。他和塞莉娜已经处惯了，常在一处消磨时光，屋里屋外都是一样。他们在范德赛德家的池塘上滑冰；同那群尖叫的小辫子丫头一起，顺着凯珀家树林通往大路的缓坡滑雪橇——橇是鲁尔夫自己攒出来的。天坏的日子就读书、用功。不光礼拜天，好些平日的晚上也是这么打发的。塞莉娜铁了心，要鲁尔夫摆脱乡下那些粗话土语；至少，也要让她在菲斯特女子精英学校里学来的那点子毛皮知识分他一半。她这个年近二十的女人，从不把这个十二岁的孩子看低一等。那孩子则说不出所以然地敬慕着她。她早早便发现，他对美有一种感觉——线条之美、质地之美、色彩与布局之美——在他这年纪的孩子里实属罕见。指尖上一条缎带的滑腻；落日的橙红与玫紫；酒红色开司米裙子的褶皱；一句话出口时的抑扬顿挫，都会在他脸上唤起一种神情，叫她吃惊。她有一册翻毛了边的丁尼生（Tennyson）诗集。她头一回给他念那句开头的诗行——「美丽的伊莱恩（Elaine），可爱的伊莱恩，阿斯托拉特（Astolat）的百合少女伊莱恩——」——他轻轻地惊叹了一声。她从书上抬起眼来，正看见他一双眼睛睁得大大的，又亮又莹然，嵌在那张瘦削的暗色脸上。

===Original===
“What is it, Roelf?”

===Chinese===
「怎么啦，鲁尔夫？」

===Original===
He had flushed. “I didn’t say nothing﻿—anything. Start over again how it goes, ‘Elaine﻿—’ ”

===Chinese===
他脸红了。「我什么也没——没说。再打头念一遍吧，『伊莱恩——』」

===Original===
She had begun again the fragrant lines, “Elaine the fair, Elaine the lovable﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
她又念起那些芬芳的诗行来，「美丽的伊莱恩，可爱的伊莱恩……」

===Original===
Since the gathering at Ooms’s hall he had been moody and sullen; had refused to answer when she spoke to him of his bid for her basket. Urged, he would only say, “Oh, it was just fun to make old Ooms mad.”

===Chinese===
自从奥姆斯厅堂那场聚会，他就又躁又闷；她跟他提起他竞买她那只盒子的事，他只管不答。逼问得急了，他也只一句：「嗐，逗逗老奥姆斯罢了。」

===Original===
Now, with the advent of Pervus DeJong, Roelf presented that most touching and miserable of spectacles, a small boy jealous and helpless in his jealousy. Selina had asked him to join the tri-weekly evening lessons; had, indeed, insisted that he be a pupil in the class round the parlour stove. Maartje had said, on the night of Pervus DeJong’s first visit, “Roelf, you sit, too, and learn. Is good for you to learn out of books the way teacher says.” Klaas Pool, too, had approved the plan, since it would cost nothing and, furthermore, would in no way interfere with Roelf’s farm work. “Sure; learn,” he said, with a large gesture.

===Chinese===
如今珀维斯·德容一露头，鲁尔夫便摆出那副最叫人心酸、最可怜的景象：一个小男孩吃着干醋，又拿这醋意毫无办法。塞莉娜邀他来上这每礼拜三回的夜课；何止邀，简直是要定他做客厅炉边那个班上的学生。珀维斯·德容头一回来的那个晚上，玛尔切便发话了：「鲁尔夫，你也坐下，学。照老师讲的，从书本里学出个好处来。」克拉斯·普尔也赞成这主意，一来不破费一个子儿，二来也绝不碍着鲁尔夫的农活。「行；学嘛。」他说，手一挥，好大的气派。

===Original===
Roelf would not. He behaved very badly; slammed doors, whistled, scuffled on the kitchen floor, made many mysterious trips through the parlour up the stairs that led off that room, ascending with a clatter; incited Geertje and Jozina to quarrels and tears; had the household in a hubbub; stumbled over Dunder, the dog, so that that anguished animal’s yelps were added to the din.

===Chinese===
鲁尔夫偏不。他闹得实在不像话：摔门，吹口哨，在厨房地板上蹭出溜儿，一趟趟神神秘秘地穿过客厅、上那屋里岔出去的楼梯，上去时踩得咚咚山响；撺掇得海尔切和约西娜又吵嘴又掉泪；搅得一家子鸡飞狗跳；还专绊那条狗邓德尔（Dunder），害那可怜的畜生一声声哀叫，也给这片喧腾添上一股。

===Original===
Selina was frantic. Lessons were impossible amidst this uproar. “It has never been like this before,” she assured Pervus, almost tearfully. “I don’t know what’s the matter. It’s awful.”

===Chinese===
塞莉娜急疯了。这么一锅沸反里课是没法上的。「从前从来不这样的，」她跟珀维斯分辩，差不多带了哭腔。「不知他是怎么了。糟透了。」

===Original===
Pervus had looked up from his slate. His eyes were calm, his lips smiling. “Is all right. In my house is too still, evenings. Next time it goes better. You see.”

===Chinese===
珀维斯从石板上抬起眼来。目光平静，嘴角带笑。「没什么。我家晚上太静。下回就好了。你瞧着。」

===Original===
Next time it did go better. Roelf disappeared into his work-shed after supper; did not emerge until after DeJong’s departure.

===Chinese===
下回果然就好了。鲁尔夫一吃过晚饭就钻进作坊没了影，直到德容走了才出来。

===Original===
There was something about the sight of this great creature bent laboriously over a slate, the pencil held clumsily in his huge fingers, that moved Selina strangely. Pity wracked her. If she had known to what emotion this pity was akin she might have taken away the slate and given him a tablet, and the whole course of her life would have been different. “Poor lad,” she thought. “Poor lad.” Chided herself for being amused at his childlike earnestness.

===Chinese===
这个大块头吃力地弓在石板上、粗大的手指笨拙地捏着铅笔——这景象里有个什么，怪怪地打动着塞莉娜。怜惜把她的心绞住了。她若知道这份怜惜跟哪种情感沾着亲，她也许就会收走石板、换给他一沓写字簿，那么她一生的路数便会全然是另一个样子。「可怜的人，」她想。「可怜的人。」转过头又怪自己，不该拿他孩子气的认真劲儿取乐。

===Original===
He did not make an apt pupil, though painstaking. Usually the top draught of the stove was open, and the glow of the fire imparted to his face and head a certain roseate glory. He was very grave. His brow wore a troubled frown. Selina would go over a problem or a sentence again and again, patiently, patiently. Then, suddenly, like a hand passed over his face, his smile would come, transforming it. He had white strong teeth, too small, and perhaps not so white as they seemed because of his russet blondeur. He would smile like a child, and Selina should have been warned by the warm rush of joy that his smile gave her. She would smile, too. He was as pleased as though he had made a fresh and wonderful discovery.

===Chinese===
他算不得一点就通的学生，尽管肯下死力气。炉子的上风门通常开着，火光给他的脸和头顶镀上一层玫瑰色的光晕。他非常郑重。眉心蹙着一团愁结。一道题、一句话，塞莉娜会一遍又一遍地过，耐心，耐心。忽然，像一只手从脸上抚过去，他的笑来了，整张脸都变了。他有一口结实的小白牙，兴许因为那一头金棕的须发，牙未必有看上去那么白。他笑起来像个孩子，而这一笑在她心里激起的那股热腾腾的欢喜，本该叫她有所警觉的。她也会跟着笑。他那个得意劲儿，仿佛又做出了一桩新鲜的、了不起的发现。

===Original===
“It’s easy,” he would say, “when you know it once.” Like a boy.

===Chinese===
「容易，」他会说，「一旦会了，就容易。」像个半大孩子。

===Original===
He usually went home by eight-thirty or nine. Often the Pools went to bed before he left. After he had gone Selina was wakeful. She would heat water and wash; brush her hair vigorously; feeling at once buoyant and depressed.

===Chinese===
他通常八点半或九点回去。普尔一家往往他还没走就先睡了。他走后，塞莉娜倒没了睡意。她会烧热水洗一洗；使劲刷一阵头发；只觉浑身轻快，又浑身沉坠。

===Original===
Sometimes they fell to talking. His wife had died in the second year of their marriage, when the child was born. The child, too, had died. A girl. He was unlucky, like that. It was the same with the farm.

===Chinese===
有时两人便聊起来。他妻子是婚后第二年死的，正赶上生孩子。孩子也没留住。一个女孩。他就是这么个背运的人。农场也一样。

===Original===
“Spring, half of the land is under water. My piece, just. Bouts’s place, next to me, is high and rich. Bouts, he don’t even need deep ploughing. His land is quick land. It warms up in the spring early. After rain it works easy. He puts in fertilizer, any kind, and his plants jump, like. My place is bad for garden truck. Wet. All the time, wet; or in summer baked before I can loosen it again. Muckland.”

===Chinese===
「一开春，一半的地泡在水里。单说我那块。挨着我的鲍茨家那块，又高又肥。鲍茨那地，连深耕都用不着。是活地。开春回暖早。雨后土一松就下得去犁。他上肥，不论什么肥，菜苗噌噌地蹿。我那块地种菜不行。湿。一年到头湿；不然夏天还没等再松一遍土，先烤成铁板一块。洼塘地。」

===Original===
Selina thought a moment. She had heard much talk between Klaas and Jakob, winter evenings. “Can’t you do something to it﻿—fix it﻿—so that the water will run off? Raise it, or dig a ditch or something?”

===Chinese===
塞莉娜想了想。冬天的晚上，克拉斯跟雅各布谈论这些，她听得多了。「你就不能想法拾掇拾掇——叫水顺顺地排掉？垫高地势，或者挖道沟什么的？」

===Original===
“We-e-ell, maybe. Maybe you could. But it costs money, draining.”

===Chinese===
「呃——兴许吧。兴许成。可排水得花钱呐。」

===Original===
“It costs money not to, doesn’t it?”

===Chinese===
「不排不也一样花钱吗？」

===Original===
He considered this, ruminatively. “Guess it does. But you don’t have to have ready cash to let the land lay. To drain it you do.”

===Chinese===
他嚼谷似的咂摸着这话。「是花。可地撂着，不用现钱；要排水，就非现钱不可。」

===Original===
Selina shook her head impatiently. “That’s a very foolish, shortsighted way to reason.”

===Chinese===
塞莉娜不耐烦地一摇头。「这么盘算，又糊涂，又近视。」

===Original===
He looked helpless as only the strong and powerful can look. Selina’s heart melted in pity. He would look down at the great calloused hands; up at her. One of the charms of Pervus DeJong lay in the things that his eyes said and his tongue did not. Women always imagined he was about to say what he looked, but he never did. It made otherwise dull conversation with him most exciting.

===Chinese===
他露出一副没抓没挪的样子——那副样子，只有强壮有力的人才摆得出。塞莉娜的心叫怜惜泡软了。他会低头瞧瞧那双老茧层叠的大手；再抬头瞧瞧她。珀维斯·德容的迷人处，其一便在他眼睛说了、舌头不说的那些话。女人们总以为他就要把眼里的话吐出口了，可他从来不。这一来，本来寡淡的谈话也变得荡人心魄。

===Original===
His was in no way a shrewd mind. His respect for Selina was almost reverence. But he had this advantage: he had married a woman, had lived with her for two years. She had borne him a child. Selina was a girl in experience. She was a woman capable of a great deal of passion, but she did not know that. Passion was a thing no woman possessed, much less talked about. It simply did not exist, except in men, and then was something to be ashamed of, like a violent temper, or a weak stomach.

===Chinese===
他这脑子，精明二字全然沾不上边。他对塞莉娜的敬重近乎敬畏。但他有一样便宜：他娶过女人，同她过了两年日子。她给他生过一个孩子。论阅历，塞莉娜还是个姑娘。她原是个蕴得下极大热情的女人，自己却不知道。热情这东西，女人家天生就没有，更别说挂在嘴上。它压根儿不存在——只存在于男人身上，而且还是桩丢人的事，好比暴脾气，好比亏虚的胃。

===Original===
By the first of March he could speak a slow, careful, and fairly grammatical English. He could master simple sums. By the middle of March the lessons would cease. There was too much work to do about the farm﻿—night work as well as day. She found herself trying not to think about the time when the lessons should cease. She refused to look ahead to April.

===Chinese===
到三月初，他已经能说一口慢悠悠、小心在意、大体合乎文法的英语。简单的算术也拿得下了。到三月中旬，课就要停了。农场上的活计太多——白天有，夜里也有。她发觉自己总在想法不去想停课的那天。她不肯往四月里看。

===Original===
One night, late in February, Selina was conscious that she was trying to control something. She was trying to keep her eyes away from something. She realized that she was trying not to look at his hands. She wanted, crazily, to touch them. She wanted to feel them about her throat. She wanted to put her lips on his hands﻿—brush the backs of them, slowly, moistly, with her mouth, lingeringly. She was terribly frightened. She thought to herself: “I am going crazy. I am losing my mind. There is something the matter with me. I wonder how I look. I must look queer.”

===Chinese===
二月末的一个晚上，塞莉娜觉出自己正拼命克制着一样东西。她正竭力把眼睛从一样东西上挪开。她明白过来了：她是死命不去看他的手。她发了疯似的想摸它们。想让它们拢上自己的喉咙。想把嘴唇贴上那双手——用嘴缓缓地、湿润地、流连忘返地拂过那手背。她吓坏了。她心里念叨：「我要疯了。我神志不清了。我身上出毛病了。不知道我这会儿是什么模样。一定一副怪相。」

===Original===
She said something to make him look up at her. His glance was mild, undismayed. So this hideous thing did not show in her face. She kept her eyes resolutely on the book. At half-past eight she closed her book suddenly. “I’m tired. I think it’s the spring coming on.” She smiled a little wavering smile. He rose and stretched himself, his great arms high above his head. Selina shivered.

===Chinese===
她没话找话地说了句什么，引他抬头看她。他的目光温和，不见慌乱。这么说，这桩可怕的东西还没显到她脸上。她死死把眼睛钉在书上。八点半，她突然合上书。「我累了。怕是春气上来了。」她笑了笑，那笑意飘飘忽忽。他站起身伸了个懒腰，两条大胳膊高高举过头顶。塞莉娜一哆嗦。

===Original===
“Two more weeks,” he said, “is the last lesson. Well, do you think I have done pretty good﻿—well?”

===Chinese===
「再过两个礼拜，」他说，「就上末一课了。呃——你看我学得还算不赖吧——呃？」

===Original===
“Very well,” Selina replied, evenly. She felt very tired.

===Chinese===
「很好。」塞莉娜平平板板地答道。她觉得累极了。

===Original===
The first week in March he was ill, and did not come. A rheumatic affliction to which he was subject. His father, old Johannes DeJong, had had it before him. Working in the wet fields did it, they said. It was the curse of the truck farmer. Selina’s evenings were free to devote to Roelf, who glowed again. She sewed, too; read; helped Mrs. Pool with the housework in a gust of sympathy and found strange relief therein; made over an old dress; studied; wrote all her letters (few enough), even one to the dried-apple aunts in Vermont. She no longer wrote to Julie Hempel. She had heard that Julie was to be married to a Kansas man named Arnold. Julie herself had not written. The first week in March passed. He did not come. Nor did he come the following Tuesday or Thursday. After a terrific battle with herself Selina, after school on Thursday, walked past his house, busily, as though bent on an errand. Despised herself for doing it, could not help herself, found a horrible and tortuous satisfaction in not looking at the house as she passed it.

===Chinese===
三月头一个礼拜，他病了，没来。是他躲不开的风湿症。他父亲老约翰内斯·德容从前就有。都说是在湿漉漉的地里做活落下的。这是种菜人的劫数。塞莉娜的晚上空了出来，全给了鲁尔夫，他又焕发了。她也做针线；读书；一阵恻然涌起，帮着普尔太太操持家务，还在里头寻着一种奇异的宽解；翻改了一条旧裙子；用功；把该回的信统统回了（本也没几封），连佛蒙特那两位蔫苹果姑母都回了一封。她不再给朱莉·亨佩尔写信了。她听说朱莉要嫁个姓阿诺德的堪萨斯人了。朱莉本人倒没来信。三月的头一个礼拜过去了。他没来。跟着礼拜二、礼拜四也没来。同自己狠狠打了一仗之后，礼拜四放了学，塞莉娜装出一副有差事在身的忙促样儿，从他门前走了过去。她一面瞧不上自己这么做，一面又由不得自己，走过那房子时偏不朝它看一眼，从中咂出一种磨人而可怕的满足。

===Original===
She was bewildered, frightened. All that week she had a curious feeling﻿—or succession of feelings. There was the sensation of suffocation followed by that of emptiness﻿—of being hollow﻿—boneless﻿—bloodless. Then, at times, there was a feeling of physical pain; at others a sense of being disembowelled. She was restless, listless, by turns. Period of furious activity followed by days of inertia. It was the spring, Maartje said. Selina hoped she wasn’t going to be ill. She had never felt like that before. She wanted to cry. She was irritable to the point of waspishness with the children in the schoolroom.

===Chinese===
她又惶惑，又害怕。那一整礼拜，她都有一种古怪的感觉——或者说一连串的感觉。先是一阵窒息，接着是空虚——空了心——没了骨头——没了血。有时是一种实实在在的疼；有时又像叫人剜去了五脏。她一阵阵地烦躁，又一阵阵地打不起精神。发疯似的忙上一阵，接着又整天整天地委顿。是春气闹的，玛尔切说。塞莉娜只盼自己别是要病倒。她从没这样过。她想哭。对教室里那群孩子，她躁得动不动就恶声恶气。

===Original===
On Saturday﻿—the fourteenth of March﻿—he walked in at seven. Klaas, Maartje, and Roelf had driven off to a gathering at Low Prairie, leaving Selina with the pigtails and old Jakob. She had promised to make taffy for them, and was in the midst of it when his knock sounded at the kitchen door. All the blood in her body rushed to her head; pounded there hotly. He entered. There slipped down over her a complete armour of calmness, of self-possession; of glib how do you do Mr. DeJong and how are you feeling and won’t you sit down and there’s no fire in the parlour we’ll have to sit here.

===Chinese===
礼拜六——三月十四号——他七点钟走了进来。克拉斯、玛尔切和鲁尔夫赶车到低草原赴会去了，家里撇下塞莉娜、两个小辫子丫头和老雅各布。她应承过给她们熬太妃糖，正做到一半，他的叩门声在厨房门上响起来。她浑身的血轰一声涌上头顶，在那里突突地烧。他进来了。一副全套的甲胄顺着她全身滑落下来，罩了个严实——镇定，自持；一套溜滑的你好啊德容先生、身子骨可大好了、快请坐吧、客厅没生火咱们只好坐这厢。

===Original===
He took part in the taffy pulling. Selina wondered if Geertje and Jozina would ever have done squealing. It was half-past eight before she bundled them off to bed with a plate of clipped taffy lozenges between them. She heard them scuffling and scrimmaging about in the rare freedom of their parents’ absence.

===Chinese===
拉糖他也上手。塞莉娜直纳闷，海尔切和约西娜还有没有个尖叫够的时候。好容易挨到八点半，她才把两人撵上床去，床当中搁了一盘剪成小块的太妃糖。她听见她们趁着爹娘难得不在家的自在，在被窝里滚作一团厮闹。

===Original===
“Now, children!” she called. “You know what you promised your mother and father.”

===Chinese===
「好了，孩子们！」她叫道。「你们应承过爹妈什么来着。」

===Original===
She heard Geertje’s tones mimicking her mincingly, “You know what you promised your mother and father.” Then a cascade of smothered giggles.

===Chinese===
她听见海尔切捏着嗓子拿腔拿调地学她：「你们应承过爹妈什么来着。」跟着一串捂在被窝里的咯咯笑。

===Original===
Pervus had been to town, evidently, for he now took from his coat pocket a bag containing half a dozen bananas﻿—that delicacy of delicacies to the farm palate. She half peeled two and brought them in to the pigtails. They ate them thickly rapturous, and dropped off to sleep immediately, surfeited.

===Chinese===
珀维斯分明进过城，因为这会儿他从外衣口袋里掏出一纸兜香蕉——庄稼人口味里的珍馐之王。她剥开两根，端进去给小辫子们。她们吃得如痴如醉、酣畅淋漓，一撑着便立时睡死过去。

===Original===
Pervus DeJong and Selina sat at the kitchen table, their books spread out before them on the oilcloth. The sweet heavy scent of the fruit filled the room. Selina brought the parlour lamp into the kitchen, the better to see. It was a nickel-bellied lamp with a yellow glass shade that cast a mellow golden glow.

===Chinese===
珀维斯·德容和塞莉娜在厨房桌边坐下，书本在油布上摊开。果子的甜香沉沉地灌满一屋。塞莉娜把客厅的灯端进厨房，好看得分明些。那是一盏镍肚子的灯，黄玻璃罩子，洒下一片温软的金光。

===Original===
“You didn’t go to the meeting,” primly. “Mr. and Mrs. Pool went.”

===Chinese===
「你没去赴会，」她说得端端正正。「普尔先生和普尔太太可去了。」

===Original===
“No. No, I didn’t go.”

===Chinese===
「没去。没，我没去。」

===Original===
“Why not?”

===Chinese===
「怎么没去？」

===Original===
She saw him swallow. “I got through too late. I went to town, and I got through too late. We’re fixing to sow tomato seeds in the hotbeds tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
她看见他咽了口唾沫。「收工收得太晚了。我进了趟城，收工收得太晚了。明儿个要往温床里点西红柿籽。」

===Original===
Selina opened McBride’s *Grammar*. “Ahem!” a school-teacherly cough. “Now, then, we’ll parse this sentence: Blucher arrived on the field of Waterloo just as Wellington was receiving the last onslaught of Napoleon. ‘Just’ may be treated as a modifier of the dependent clause. That is: ‘Just’ means: at the time *at which*. Well. *Just* here modified *at the time*. And Wellington is the﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
塞莉娜翻开麦克布莱德《语法》。「咳嗯！」一声小学教师式的咳嗽。「来，咱们把这句解析一下：布吕歇尔（Blucher）赶到滑铁卢（Waterloo）战场，正当威灵顿（Wellington）挨着拿破仑（Napoleon）的最后一击。『正当』可以看作从属从句的一个修饰语。也就是说：『正当』的意思是：在*那个*时刻。好。这里的『正当』，修饰的是*那个时刻*。威灵顿是……」

===Original===
This for half an hour. Selina kept her eyes resolutely on the book. His voice went on with the dry business of parsing and its deep resonance struck a response from her as a harp responds when a hand is swept over its strings. Upstairs she could hear old Jakob clumping about in his preparations for bed. Then there was only stillness overhead. Selina kept her eyes resolutely on the book. Yet she saw, as though her eyes rested on them, his large, strong hands. On the backs of them was a fine golden down that deepened at his wrists. Heavier and darker at the wrists. She found herself praying a little for strength﻿—for strength against this horror and wickedness. This sin, this abomination that held her. A terrible, stark, and pitiful prayer, couched in the idiom of the Bible.

===Chinese===
这样折腾了半个钟头。塞莉娜死死把眼睛钉在书上。他的声音干巴巴地一路解析下去，那深沉的共鸣却在她身上拨出应和，像一只手扫过琴弦，竖琴便铮然作响。楼上，她听得见老雅各布咚咚咚地走动，拾掇着睡下。后来头顶上只剩一片寂静。塞莉娜死死把眼睛钉在书上。可她仍旧看得见——就像眼睛真落在那上头——他那双大手、那双结实的手。手背上覆着一层细细的金色茸毛，到腕子上便深起来。腕子上，更浓，更深。她发觉自己在小声祷告，求力量——求一股力量，顶住这场可怕的邪祟。这罪孽，这攫住她的可憎之物。一篇赤裸裸的、可怕的、可怜见的祷告，用的全是《圣经》的腔调。

===Original===
“Oh, God, keep my eyes and my thoughts away from him. Away from his hands. Let me keep my eyes and my thoughts away from the golden hairs on his wrists. Let me not think of his wrists.﻿ ﻿… The owner of the southwest ¼ sells a strip 20 rods wide along the south side of his farm. How much does he receive at $150 per acre?”

===Chinese===
「哦，上帝，叫我的眼睛、我的心念都离开他。离开他的手。叫我守住眼睛、守住心念，别看别想他腕子上的金毛。叫我不想他的腕子。……西南四分之一地块的主人，沿农场南边卖掉一条宽 20 杆的地。一英亩 150 块，他能得多少钱？」

===Original===
He triumphed in this transaction, began the struggle with the square root of 576. Square roots agonized him. She washed the slate clean with her little sponge. He was leaning close in his effort to comprehend the fiendish little figures that marched so tractably under Selina’s masterly pencil.

===Chinese===
这笔账他打了个大胜仗，接着开始同 576 的平方根较劲。平方根最叫他受罪。她用她那块小海绵把石板揩干净。他凑得近近的，拼命要弄懂那些小恶魔似的数字——它们在塞莉娜笔下训练有素的铅笔底下，走得多么驯顺。

===Original===
She took it up, glibly. “The remainder must contain twice the product of the tens by the units plus the square of the units.” He blinked. Utterly bewildered. “*And*,” went on Selina, blithely, “twice the tens, times the units, plus the square of the units, is the same as the sum of twice the tens, and the units, times the units. *Therefore*”﻿—with a flourish﻿—“add 4 units to the 40 and multiply the result by 4. *Therefore*”﻿—in final triumph﻿—“the square root of 576 is 24.”

===Chinese===
她接过话头，口齿溜利。「余数必定含有：十位数乘个位数之积的两倍，加个位数的平方。」他直眨眼。全然懵了。「*而且*，」塞莉娜轻快地接下去，「十位的两倍乘个位，加个位的平方，等于十位两倍加个位之和，再乘个位。*所以*」——笔锋潇洒地一挥——「40 加上 4 个单位，再把结果乘以 4。*所以*」——最后凯旋式地一顿——「576 的平方根是 24。」

===Original===
She was breathing rather fast. The fire in the kitchen stove snapped and cracked. “Now, then, suppose you do that for me. We’ll wipe it out. There! What must the remainder contain?”

===Chinese===
她喘得有点急。厨房炉子里的火噼啪爆响。「来，这回你自己给我做一遍。咱们把它揩掉。好！余数必定含有什么？」

===Original===
He took it up, slowly, haltingly. The house was terribly still except for the man’s voice. “The remainder﻿ ﻿… twice﻿ ﻿… product﻿ ﻿… tens﻿ ﻿… units﻿ ﻿…” A something in his voice﻿—a note﻿—a timbre. She felt herself swaying queerly, as though the whole house were gently rocking. Little delicious agonizing shivers chased each other, hot and cold, up her arms, down her legs, over her spine.﻿ ﻿… “plus the square of the units is the same as the sum twice the tens﻿ ﻿… twice﻿ ﻿… the tens﻿ ﻿… the tens﻿ ﻿…” His voice stopped.

===Chinese===
他接了过来，慢慢地，一顿一顿。屋子里静得怕人，只剩这个男人的声音。「余数……两倍……积……十位……个位……」他的嗓音里有个什么——一个音——一种音色。她觉得自己古怪地晃悠起来，仿佛整座房子在轻轻地摇。细细的、又甜又熬人的战栗你追我赶，一阵热一阵凉，顺着胳膊往上爬，顺着腿往下溜，滚过脊梁。……「加个位的平方，等于十位两倍之和……两倍……十位……十位……」他的声音停了。

===Original===
Selina’s eyes leaped from the book to his hands, uncontrollably. Something about them startled her. They were clenched into fists. Her eyes now leaped from those clenched fists to the face of the man beside her. Her head came up, and back. Her wide startled eyes met his. His were a blaze of blinding blue in his tanned face. Some corner of her mind that was still working clearly noted this. Then his hands unclenched. The blue blaze scorched her, enveloped her. Her cheek knew the harsh cool feel of a man’s cheek. She sensed the potent, terrifying, pungent odour of close contact﻿—a mixture of tobacco smoke, his hair, freshly laundered linen, an indefinable body smell. It was a mingling that disgusted and attracted her. She was at once repelled and drawn. Then she felt his lips on hers and her own, incredibly, responding eagerly, wholly to that pressure.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜的眼睛从书上跳到他的手上，怎么也管不住。那双手上有个什么，唬了她一跳。它们攥成了两只拳头。她的眼睛又从那两只攥紧的拳头跳到身旁这个男人的脸上。她的头扬起来，向后仰去。她一双惊惶的大眼睛撞上了他的眼睛。他的那双，在晒黑的脸上，是一团耀得人睁不开眼的蓝色的火。她脑子里还有一角清明的地方，把这一笔记了下来。然后他的手松开了。那团蓝火灼着她，裹住她。她的脸颊触到一个男人脸颊那粗砺而微凉的触感。她嗅到贴身亲近时那股强烈的、骇人的、呛人的气味——烟叶、他的头发、刚浆洗过的亚麻衬衣，还有一股说不清的体味，混作一气。这混和叫她作呕，又叫她心醉。她一面被推拒开，一面被拽拢去。随后她觉出他的唇压上她的唇，而她自己的唇，难以置信地，急切地、整个地回应着那一压。

## VII / 第七章

===Original===
They were married the following May, just two months later. The High Prairie school year practically ended with the appearance of the first tender shoots of green that meant onions, radishes, and spinach above the rich sandy loam. Selina’s classes broke, dwindled, shrank to almost nothing. The school became a kindergarten of five-year-old babies who wriggled and shifted and scratched in the warm spring air that came from the teeming prairie through the open windows. The schoolhouse stove stood rusty-red and cold. The drum in Selina’s bedroom was a black genie deprived of his power now to taunt her.

===Chinese===
第二年五月，只隔了两个月，他们就成婚了。高草原的学年，几乎是随着最初那一片娇嫩绿芽的出现便告了终结——那绿芽意味着洋葱、小萝卜和菠菜已顶破了肥沃的沙壤。塞莉娜班上的学生散的散、走的走，缩到几乎不剩什么人。学校成了一所幼儿园，全是五岁的娃娃，在暖融融的春日空气里扭来扭去、蹭来蹭去、抓挠个不停——那空气正从生机蓬勃的草原上，透过敞开的窗户涌进来。校舍里的火炉立在那儿，冷冰冰的，锈成了暗红色。塞莉娜卧室里那只形如鼓的取暖筒，如今成了一只被夺去法力的黑妖精，再不能嘲弄她了。

===Original===
Selina was at once bewildered and calm; rebellious and content. Overlaying these emotions was something like grim amusement. Beneath them, something like fright. High Prairie, in May, was green and gold and rose and blue. The spring flowers painted the fields and the roadside with splashes of yellow, of pink, of mauve, and purple. Violets, buttercups, mandrakes, marsh-marigolds, hepatica. The air was soft and cool from the lake. Selina had never known spring in the country before. It made her ache with an actual physical ache. She moved with a strange air of fatality. It was as if she were being drawn inexorably, against her will, her judgment, her plans, into something sweet and terrible. When with Pervus she was elated, gay, voluble. He talked little; looked at her dumbly, worshippingly. When he brought her a withered bunch of trilliums, the tears came to her eyes. He had walked to Updike’s woods to get them because he had heard her say she loved them, and there were none nearer. They were limp and listless from the heat, and from being held in his hand. He looked up at her from where he stood on the kitchen steps, she in the doorway. She took them, laid her hand on his head. It was as when some great gentle dog brings in a limp and bedraggled prize dug from the yard and, laying it at one’s feet, looks up at one with soft asking eyes.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜一时迷惘，一时安恬；一时叛逆，一时知足。这些情绪之上，覆着一层近乎冷酷的自嘲；这些情绪之下，潜着一丝近乎惊惶的惧意。五月的高草原，绿一块，金一块，玫瑰红一块，天蓝一块。春花把田野和路边泼洒上一片片的黄、粉、淡紫与深紫。紫罗兰、毛茛、曼德拉草、驴蹄草、獐耳细辛。湖上吹来的空气温柔而清凉。塞莉娜从前从不曾见识过乡野的春天。它使她发疼——一种真真切切、肉体上的疼。她一举一动都带着一种听天由命的奇怪神气，仿佛正被一种不由分说的力量拽着，违背她的意志、她的判断、她的筹划，拖进某种既甜蜜又可怕的东西里去。和珀维斯在一起时，她欢畅、快活、滔滔不绝。他话很少，只是哑然望着她，满眼崇敬。那天他给她带来一束蔫了的延龄草（trilliums），她眼里顿时涌上了泪。他是走着到厄普代克（Updike）家的树林里去采的，只因听她说过她爱这花，而近处又没有。花被暑气蒸的、又被他攥在手里的，蔫软没了精神。他站在厨房台阶下仰头望她，她立在门口。她接过花，把手按在他头上。就像一只高大温顺的狗从院子里刨出一件湿漉漉的战利品叼进屋来，放在人脚边，抬起头，用温柔而探问的眼睛望着人。

===Original===
There were days when the feeling of unreality possessed her. She, a truck farmer’s wife, living in High Prairie the rest of her days! Why, no! No! Was this the great adventure that her father had always spoken of? She, who was going to be a happy wayfarer down the path of life﻿—any one of a dozen things. This High Prairie winter was to have been only an episode. Not her life! She looked at Maartje. Oh, but she’d never be like that. That was stupid, unnecessary. Pink and blue dresses in the house, for her. Frills on the window curtains. Flowers in bowls.

===Chinese===
有些日子，一种恍如梦中的不真实感会攫住她。她，一个蔬菜农场主的妻子，要在高草原终此一生！嘿，不！不！这难道就是她父亲成天挂在嘴上的那场大冒险？她，本要做人生路上一名快乐的行脚人——十二样营生里随挑一样。高草原的这个冬天，本该只是一段插曲。断不是她的一生！她望着玛尔切。哦，可她绝不会变成那样。那太蠢了，大可不必。她屋里要挂粉的蓝的裙子。窗帘上要缀荷叶边。碗里要插鲜花。

===Original===
Some of the pangs and terrors with which most prospective brides are assailed she confided to Mrs. Pool while that active lady was slamming about the kitchen.

===Chinese===
大多数准新娘都会遭到种种痛楚与恐惧的袭击，其中的几样，塞莉娜向普尔太太吐露了——那时这位手脚麻利的主妇正在厨房里摔摔打打，锅碗瓢盆地乒乓作响。

===Original===
“Did you ever feel scared and﻿—and sort of﻿—scared when you thought about marry, Mrs. Pool?”

===Chinese===
「普尔太太，您想到要结婚了的时候，心里怕不怕——怕那种——那种说不清道不明的怕？」

===Original===
Maartje Pool’s hands were in a great batch of bread dough which she pummelled and slapped and kneaded vigorously. She shook out a handful of flour on the baking board while she held the dough mass in the other hand, then plumped it down and again began to knead, both hands doubled into fists.

===Chinese===
玛尔切·普尔的两只手正埋在一大团发面里，又是捶，又是拍，又是使劲揉。她一只手托着面团，另一只手往面板上抖撒一把干面，随后把面团噗地一撂，重新揉起，两只手都攥成了拳头。

===Original===
She laughed a short little laugh. “I ran away.”

===Chinese===
她短短地笑了一声。「我逃跑来着。」

===Original===
“You did! You mean you really ran﻿—but why? Didn’t you lo﻿—like Klaas?”

===Chinese===
「您跑了！您是说您当真跑过——可为什么呀？难道您不爱——不喜欢克拉斯吗？」

===Original===
Maartje Pool kneaded briskly, the colour high in her cheeks, what with the vigorous pummelling and rolling, and something else that made her look strangely young for the moment﻿—girlish, almost. “Sure I liked him, I liked him.”

===Chinese===
玛尔切·普尔手上揉得起劲，两颊红扑扑的——一半是捶揉擀轧的力气活儿闹的，一半是别的什么，一时竟让她显得出奇地年轻，几乎像个大姑娘。「我当然喜欢他，我是喜欢他的。」

===Original===
“But you ran away?”

===Chinese===
「可您还是跑了？」

===Original===
“Not far. I came back. Nobody ever knew I ran, even. But I ran. I knew.”

===Chinese===
「没跑远。我又回来了。连我跑过这件事，都没有一个人知道。可我到底是跑了。我心里有数。」

===Original===
“Why did you come back?”

===Chinese===
「那您为什么又回来了？」

===Original===
Maartje elucidated her philosophy without being in the least aware that it could be called by any such high-sounding name. “You can’t run away far enough. Except you stop living you can’t run away from life.”

===Chinese===
玛尔切阐述了她的人生哲学，却全然不觉得这套东西配得上这么个响亮的名目。「你逃不出多远。除非你不活了，谁也没法从生活里逃出去。」

===Original===
The girlish look had fled. She was world-old. Her strong arms ceased their pounding and thumping for a moment. On the steps just outside Klaas and Jakob were scanning the weekly reports preparatory to going into the city late that afternoon.

===Chinese===
大姑娘的神气已经遁走。她苍老得阅尽尘世。那两条结实的胳膊，捶打拍击停了片刻。门外台阶上，克拉斯和雅各布正在细看每周的行情报表，预备那天傍晚晚些时候进城。

===Original===
Selina had the difficult task of winning Roelf to her all over again. He was like a trusting little animal, who, wounded by the hand he has trusted, is shy of it. She used blandishments on this boy of thirteen such as she had never vouchsafed the man she was going to marry. He had asked her, bluntly, one day: “Why are you going to marry with him?” He never spoke the name.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜碰上一桩难事：要重新把鲁尔夫赢回来。他像一头信人的小动物，被它信惯了的那只手伤了，从此见了那只手就躲。她对这个十三岁的男孩使出的哄劝手段，是从不肯施与那个她要嫁的男人的。有一天他直统统地问她：「你干吗要嫁给他？」那个名字他从来不说。

===Original===
She thought deeply. What to say? The answer ready on her tongue would have little meaning for this boy. There came to her a line from Lancelot and Elaine. She answered, “To serve him, and to follow him through the world.” She thought that rather fine-sounding until Roelf promptly rejected it. “That’s no reason. An answer out of a book. Anyway, to follow him through the world is dumb. He stays right here in High Prairie all his life.”

===Chinese===
她深深地想了想。说什么好呢？舌尖上现成的那套回答，对这男孩不会有多大意思。她想起《兰斯洛特与伊莱恩》（Lancelot and Elaine）里的一句诗，便答道：「伺候他，跟他走遍天涯。」她还觉得这话挺堂皇，不料鲁尔夫立刻驳了回来：「这算不得理由。书本里搬来的答案。再说，跟他走遍天涯也实在傻。他一辈子就钉死在高草原这儿不动窝。」

===Original===
“How do you know!” Selina retorted, almost angrily. Startled, too.

===Chinese===
「你怎么知道！」塞莉娜回嘴道，几乎是动了气。也吓了一跳。

===Original===
“I know. He stays.”

===Chinese===
「我知道。他就是不走。」

===Original===
Still, he could not withstand her long. Together they dug and planted flower beds in Pervus’s dingy front yard. It was too late for tulips now. Pervus had brought her some seeds from town. They ranged all the way from poppies to asters; from purple iris to morning glories. The last named were to form the back-porch vine, of course, because they grew quickly. Selina, city-bred, was ignorant of varieties, but insisted she wanted an old-fashioned garden﻿—marigolds, pinks, mignonette, phlox. She and Roelf dug, spaded, planted. The DeJong place was markedly ugly even in that community of squat houses. It lacked the air of sparkling cleanliness that saved the other places from sordidness. The house, even then, was thirty years old﻿—a gray, weather-beaten frame box with a mansard roof and a flat face staring out at the dense willows by the roadside. It needed paint; the fences sagged; the window curtains were awry. The parlour was damp, funereal. The old woman who tended the house for Pervus slopped about all day with a pail and a wet gray rag. There was always a crazy campanile of dirty dishes stacked on the table, and the last meal seemed never to catch up with the next. About the whole house there was a starkness, a bareness that proclaimed no woman who loved it dwelt therein.

===Chinese===
不过，他到底拗不过她多久。两人一起在珀维斯那灰扑扑的前院里掘土，辟出花床来栽花。如今种郁金香是太迟了。珀维斯从镇上给她捎回来一些花籽，从罂粟到紫菀，从紫鸢尾到牵牛花，应有尽有。末一种照例要拿来爬后廊的廊柱，因为牵牛花长得快。塞莉娜城里长大，不谙花草品类，却一口咬定要一座老式花园——金盏花、石竹、木樨草、福禄考。她和鲁尔夫掘的掘，铲的铲，种的种。德容家的宅子，即便在那一带矮墩墩的农舍中间，也丑得出奇。别家宅子靠一股亮堂洁净的气派才免于寒碜，这宅子偏没有。房子那时就已经三十年了——一只灰扑扑、经风历雨的木头匣子，顶着复折式屋顶，扁塌塌的正面怔怔地望着路边的密柳。它该刷漆了；栅栏歪斜着；窗帘挂得七扭八歪。客厅潮湿，像座灵堂。替珀维斯照看宅子的那个老太太，整天拎一只桶、一块湿灰抹布，噗嗤噗嗤地拖来拖去。桌上总垒着一座摇摇欲坠的钟塔，塔身全是脏盘子，上一顿饭仿佛永远赶不上下一顿。整座宅子透着一股荒凉、一股空落，明明白白宣告：这里面住的，没有一个爱它的女人。

===Original===
Selina told herself (and Pervus) that she would change all that. She saw herself going about with a brush and a can of white paint, leaving beauty in her wake, where ugliness had been.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜对自己（也对珀维斯）说，这一切她都要改掉。她看见自己提着一把刷子、一桶白漆在屋里屋外走动，身后留下美，原先那里是丑。

===Original===
Her trousseau was of the scantiest. Pervus’s household was already equipped with such linens as they would need. The question of a wedding gown troubled her until Maartje suggested that she be married in the old Dutch wedding dress that lay in the bride’s chest in Selina’s bedroom.

===Chinese===
她的嫁妆少得可怜。珀维斯家里的被褥桌布之类一应用品早已齐备。婚纱的事让她犯了愁，直到玛尔切提议，就穿那身荷兰老式新娘礼服成婚——衣服就收在塞莉娜卧室里的荷兰新娘箱中。

===Original===
“A real Dutch bride,” Maartje said. “Your man will think that is fine.” Pervus was delighted. Selina basked in his love like a kitten in the sun. She was, after all, a very lonely little bride with only two photographs on the shelf in her bedroom to give her courage and counsel. The old Dutch wedding gown was many inches too large for her. The skirt-band overlapped her slim waist; her slender little bosom did not fill out the generous width of the bodice; but the effect of the whole was amazingly quaint as well as pathetic. The wings of the stiffly embroidered coif framed the white face from which the eyes looked out, large and dark. She had even tried to wear the hand-carved shoes, but had to give that up. In them her feet were as lost as minnows in a rowboat. She had much difficulty with the queer old buttons and fastenings. It was as though the dead and gone Sophia Kroon were trying, with futile ghostly fingers, to prevent this young thing from meeting the fate that was to be hers.

===Chinese===
「地道的一位荷兰新娘，」玛尔切说。「你男人准觉得体面。」珀维斯喜不自胜。塞莉娜沐浴在他的爱里，像晒着太阳的小猫。她毕竟是个十分孤单的小新娘，卧室搁板上只有两张照片给她壮胆、给她出主意。那身荷兰老式礼服，她穿上大了好几寸：裙腰在她纤细的腰上叠出一圈富余；她小小的、瘦瘦的胸脯，撑不起衣襟那宽绰的幅面；然而通身的效果却别致得出奇，也凄婉得出奇。硬绣出双翼的头巾框住那张白脸，一双眼睛又大又黑，从脸上望出来。她连那双手工木鞋都试过，也只得作罢——脚搁在里面，就像掉进划艇里的小鱼苗，找不着东南西北。那些古里古怪的旧纽扣和搭扣也让她费了好一番手脚。仿佛死去多年的索菲娅·克龙正用一双徒劳的、幽灵似的手指，拦着这年轻的生命，不许她去赴那注定属于她的命运。

===Original===
They were married at the Pools’. Klaas and Maartje had insisted on furnishing the wedding supper﻿—ham, chickens, sausages, cakes, pickles, beer. The Reverend Dekker married them and all through the ceremony Selina chided herself because she could not keep her mind on his words in the fascination of watching his short stubby beard as it waggled with every motion of his jaw. Pervus looked stiff, solemn, and uncomfortable in his wedding blacks﻿—not at all the handsome giant of the everyday corduroys and blue shirt. In the midst of the ceremony Selina had her moment of panic when she actually saw herself running shrieking from this company, this man, this house, down the road, on, on toward﻿—toward what? The feeling was so strong that she was surprised to find herself still standing there in the Dutch wedding gown answering “I do” in the proper place.

===Chinese===
婚礼在普尔家举行。喜宴——火腿、鸡、香肠、糕饼、腌菜、啤酒——由克拉斯和玛尔切做主置办。德克尔牧师为他们证婚。整个仪式过程中，塞莉娜一直在暗暗责怪自己：牧师下颌每动一下，他那部又短又粗的胡子便跟着一颤一颤，她竟看得出了神，心神怎么也拢不到他的话上去。珀维斯穿着一身娶亲的黑礼服，僵硬、板正、浑身不自在——全不是平日灯芯绒裤、蓝布衬衫里那个漂亮的巨人。仪式进行到中途，塞莉娜有一刻惊慌发作，当真看见自己尖叫着逃离这群人、这个男人、这座房子，顺着大路跑啊，跑啊，跑向——跑向什么？那感觉强烈得很，以至于发现自己竟还穿着那身荷兰新娘礼服站在原地、在该应答的地方应了一声「我愿意」时，她自己也吃了一惊。

===Original===
The wedding gifts were few. The Pools had given them a “hanging lamp,” coveted of the farmer’s wife; a hideous atrocity in yellow, with pink roses on its shade and prisms dangling and tinkling all around the edge. It was intended to hang suspended from the parlour ceiling, and worked up and down on a sort of pulley chain. From the Widow Paarlenberg came a water set in red frosted glass shading to pink﻿—a fat pitcher and six tumblers. Roelf’s gift, the result of many weeks’ labour in the work-shed, was a bride’s chest copied from the fine old piece that had saved Selina’s room from sheer ugliness. He had stained the wood, polished it. Had carved the front of it with her initials﻿—very like those that stood out so boldly on the old chest upstairs﻿—S. P. D. And the year﻿—1890. The whole was a fine piece of craftsmanship for a boy of thirteen﻿—would not have discredited a man of any age. It was the one beautiful gift among Selina’s clumsy crude wedding things. She had thanked him with tears in her eyes. “Roelf, you’ll come to see me often, won’t you? Often!” Then, as he had hesitated, “I’ll need you so. You’re all I’ve got.” A strange thing for a bride to say.

===Chinese===
贺仪寥寥。普尔家送的是一盏「吊灯」，那是庄稼人的媳妇人人艳羡之物——黄色的丑东西一件，灯罩上一朵朵粉玫瑰，边上垂挂一圈玻璃棱片，叮叮当当。这灯原是要吊在客厅天棚下的，靠一根滑轮链子升上降下。帕伦贝格寡妇送来一套磨砂玻璃水具，红色晕成粉色——一把肥墩墩的凉水壶，六只直筒杯。鲁尔夫的礼物，是他在工棚里熬了好几个礼拜的活计：一只荷兰新娘箱，仿的是楼上那件老家什——正是它，才救了塞莉娜的卧室，使它不至于丑到底。他把木料上了色，打磨得发亮，箱子正面雕上她姓名的起首字母——同楼上老箱子上赫然凸出的那几个几乎如出一辙——S. P. D.，还有年份——1890。放在一个十三岁的男孩身上，这是件漂亮的手艺——多大岁数的汉子拿出来也不跌份儿。在塞莉娜那些粗笨寒碜的新婚物件中间，这是唯一一件美的东西。她含着泪谢了他。「鲁尔夫，你会常来看我的，对不对？要常来！」见他犹豫，又道：「我多需要你啊。我有的，就只是你了。」这话出自一位新娘之口，未免蹊跷。

===Original===
“I’ll come,” the boy had said, trying to make his voice casual, his tone careless. “Sure, I’ll come oncet in a while.”

===Chinese===
「我会来的，」男孩当时说，竭力让嗓音显得随口，语气显得满不在乎。「那当然，我隔三差五会来上一遭。」

===Original===
“Once, Roelf. *Once* in a while.”

===Chinese===
「一趟，鲁尔夫。隔三差五来*一趟*。」

===Original===
He repeated it after her, dutifully.

===Chinese===
他便乖乖地跟着她念了一遍。

===Original===
After the wedding they went straight to DeJong’s house. In May the vegetable farmer cannot neglect his garden even for a day. The house had been made ready for them. The sway of the old housekeeper was over. Her kitchen bedroom was empty.

===Chinese===
婚礼一完，两人径直回了德容家。五月里，种菜的人连一天也撂不下他的园子。屋子已为他们拾掇停当。看屋老太太的统治告终了。她那间挨着厨房的卧房空了出来。

===Original===
Throughout the supper Selina had had thoughts which were so foolish and detached as almost to alarm her.

===Chinese===
整顿晚饭期间，塞莉娜心里转着些念头，又傻气、又心不在焉，几乎把她自己吓一跳。

===Original===
“Now I am married. I am Mrs. Pervus DeJong. That’s a pretty name. It would look quite distinguished on a calling card, very spidery and fine:

===Chinese===
「如今我嫁人了。我是珀维斯·德容太太。这名字好听。印在名片上一定很气派，蛛丝一样的细花字：

===Original===
Mrs. Pervus DeJong

===Chinese===
珀维斯·德容太太

===Original===
At Home Fridays

===Chinese===
每逢周五在家会客

===Original===
She recalled this later, grimly, when she was Mrs. Pervus DeJong, at home not only Fridays, but Saturdays, Sundays, Mondays, Tuesdays, Wednesdays, and Thursdays.

===Chinese===
后来她冷冷地记起这一节，那时她已是珀维斯·德容太太，在家会客的日子何止周五——周六、周日、周一、周二、周三、周四，天天都在家。

===Original===
They drove down the road to DeJong’s place. Selina thought, “Now I am driving home with my husband. I feel his shoulder against mine. I wish he would talk. I wish he would say something. Still, I’m not frightened.”

===Chinese===
两人赶着车沿大路往德容家去。塞莉娜想：「如今我正随丈夫回家。我觉得出他的肩膀贴着我的肩膀。真盼他说话。盼他说点什么。不过，我并不害怕。」

===Original===
Pervus’s market wagon was standing in the yard, shafts down. He should have gone to market today; would certainly have to go tomorrow, starting early in the afternoon so as to get a good stand in the Haymarket. By the light of his lantern the wagon seemed to Selina to be a symbol. She had often seen it before, but now that it was to be a part of her life﻿—this the DeJong market wagon and she Mrs. DeJong﻿—she saw clearly what a crazy, disreputable, and poverty-proclaiming old vehicle it was, in contrast with the neat strong wagon in Klaas Pool’s yard, smart with green paint and red lettering that announced, “Klaas Pool, Garden Produce.” With the two sleek farm horses the turnout looked as prosperous and comfortable as Klaas himself.

===Chinese===
珀维斯的送菜马车停在场院里，车辕头耷拉在地上。今天他本该进城赶集的；明天则非去不可，还得午后一早就动身，好在海马克特占个像样的摊位。借着提灯的光，那辆车在塞莉娜眼里成了一个象征。从前她常常见它，可如今它就要成为她生命的一部分——这就是德容家的送菜马车，而她就是德容太太——她这才看清，这是一辆多么散架、邋遢、明明白白自报着穷字的老车；比起克拉斯·普尔场院里那辆齐整结实的车来，真是天壤。普尔那辆漆着鲜亮的绿漆，红字标着「克拉斯·普尔，园圃时蔬」，配上两匹毛色油亮的农用马，整套装束显得就像克拉斯本人一样兴旺、熨帖。

===Original===
Pervus swung her down from the seat of the buggy, his hand about her waist, and held her so for a moment, close. Selina said, “You must have that wagon painted, Pervus. And the seat-springs fixed and the sideboard mended.”

===Chinese===
珀维斯把她从轻便马车的座上抱下来，一只手拢着她的腰，就这样把她贴身搂了片刻。塞莉娜说：「那辆车你可得叫人刷刷漆，珀维斯。座垫簧也得修修，栏板也得补补。」

===Original===
He stared. “Wagon!”

===Chinese===
他直瞪瞪地看着她。「车！」

===Original===
“Yes. It looks a sight.”

===Chinese===
「是啊。它可真够寒碜的。」

===Original===
The house was tidy enough, but none too clean. Old Mrs. Voorhees had not been minded to keep house too scrupulously for a man who would be unlikely to know whether or not it was clean. Pervus lighted the lamps. There was a fire in the kitchen stove. It made the house seem stuffy on this mild May night. Selina thought that her own little bedroom at the Pools’, no longer hers, must be deliciously cool and still with the breeze fanning fresh from the west. Pervus was putting the horse into the barn. The bedroom was off the sitting room. The window was shut. This last year had taught Selina to prepare the night before for next morning’s rising, so as to lose the least possible time. She did this now, unconsciously. She took off her white muslin underwear with its frills and embroidery﻿—the three stiff petticoats, and the stiffly starched corset-cover, and the high-bosomed corset and put them into the bureau drawer that she herself had cleaned and papered neatly the week before. She brushed her hair, laid out tomorrow’s garments, put on her high-necked, long-sleeved nightgown and got into this strange bed. She heard Pervus DeJong shut the kitchen door; the latch clicked, the lock turned. Heavy quick footsteps across the bare kitchen floor. This man was coming into her room﻿ ﻿… “You can’t run far enough,” Maartje Pool had said. “Except you stop living you can’t run away from life.”

===Chinese===
屋子收拾得算整洁了，却谈不上多干净。弗里斯老太太拿定了主意：反正一个男人家也分不清干净不干净，犯不上替他操持得那么精细。珀维斯点亮了灯。厨房炉子里生着火，在这温和的五月夜里，倒把屋子熏得发闷。塞莉娜想，她那间小卧室——如今已不是她的了——这会儿必定又凉爽又清静，西风正把新鲜的空气扇进去。珀维斯在牲口棚里安顿马。卧室挨着起居间。窗户关着。这最后一年教会了塞莉娜头天夜里便为第二天清早的起身备好一切，好把耗掉的时间减到最少。此刻她便不知不觉地照做了。她脱下那身缀着褶边和绣花的白色细麻内衣——三条硬衬裙、浆得笔挺的胸挡、高胸的紧身褡——收进五斗柜抽屉里；那抽屉是她自己头个礼拜才擦净、垫好新纸的。她梳了头发，摆出明天的衣裳，穿上高领长袖的睡袍，钻进这张陌生的床。她听见珀维斯·德容关上厨房门；门闩咔哒一响，锁舌转动了。光秃秃的厨房地板上，响起沉重而急促的脚步。这个男人正走进她的房间……「你逃不出多远，」玛尔切·普尔说过。「除非你不活了，谁也没法从生活里逃出去。」

===Original===
Next morning it was dark when he awakened her at four. She started up with a little cry and sat up, straining her ears, her eyes. “Is that you, Father?” She was little Selina Peake again, and Simeon Peake had come in, gay, debonair, from a night’s gaming.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，天还黑着，他四点钟便把她叫醒了。她低低叫了一声坐起来，竖起耳朵，睁大眼睛。「是您吗，爸爸？」她又变回了小小的塞莉娜·皮克——西缅·皮克刚赌了一夜，正兴致勃勃、风流倜傥地进门来。

===Original===
Pervus DeJong was already padding about the room in stocking feet. “What﻿—what time is it? What’s the matter, Father? Why are you up? Haven’t you gone to bed﻿ ﻿…” Then she remembered.

===Chinese===
珀维斯·德容已经只穿着袜子，在屋里啪嗒啪嗒地踱来踱去。「什么——什么时候了？怎么了，爸爸？您怎么起来了？您还没上床……」这时她才想起来。

===Original===
Pervus DeJong laughed and came toward her. “Get up, little lazy bones. It’s after four. All yesterday’s work I’ve got to do, and all today’s. Breakfast, little Lina, breakfast. You are a farmer’s wife now.”


===Chinese===
珀维斯·德容笑了，朝她走过来。「起来吧，小懒骨头。过了四点了。昨儿一整天的活儿我都得补上，还有今儿一整天的。做早饭去，小莉娜（Lina），做早饭。你如今是庄稼人的媳妇了。」

## VIII / 第八章

===Original===
By October High Prairie Housewives told each other that Mrs. Pervus DeJong was “expecting.” Dirk DeJong was born in the bedroom off the sitting room on the fifteenth day of March, of a bewildered, somewhat resentful, but deeply interested mother; and a proud, foolish, and vainglorious father whose air of achievement, considering the really slight part he had played in the long, tedious, and racking business, was disproportionate. The name Dirk had sounded to Selina like something tall, straight, and slim. Pervus had chosen it. It had been his grandfather’s name.
===Chinese===
到了十月，高草原的主妇们便彼此相告：珀维斯·德容太太「有喜」了。三月十五日，德克·德容在起居间隔壁那间卧室里降生；生他的母亲茫然无措、隐隐怨怼，却兴味深深；生他的父亲骄傲、痴愚、满脸得意——论他在这一桩漫长、磨人、撕心裂肺的大事里实际出的那点力，他那副成就非凡的神气未免太不相称了。「德克」这名字在塞莉娜耳中像一种又高、又直、又细瘦的东西。是珀维斯挑的。那原是他祖父的名字。

===Original===
Sometimes, during those months, Selina would look back on her first winter in High Prairie﻿—that winter of the icy bedroom, the chill black drum, the schoolhouse fire, the chilblains, the Pool pork﻿—and it seemed a lovely dream; a time of ease, of freedom, of careless happiness. That icy room had been her room; that mile of road traversed on bitter winter mornings a mere jaunt; the schoolhouse stove a toy, fractious but fascinating.
===Chinese===
那几个月里，塞莉娜有时会回望她在高草原度过的头一个冬天——冰窖似的卧室、寒气砭骨的黑「鼓」、校舍的炉火、冻疮、普尔家的猪肉——竟像一场可爱的梦；一段安逸、自在、无忧无虑的时光。那间冰冷刺骨的屋子曾是她的屋子；严冬清晨跋涉的那一里路，不过是趟闲庭信步；校舍里的火炉不过是件玩具，脾气坏，却迷人。

===Original===
Pervus DeJong loved his pretty young wife, and she him. But young love thrives on colour, warmth, beauty. It becomes prosaic and inarticulate when forced to begin its day at four in the morning by reaching blindly, dazedly, for limp and obscure garments dangling from bedpost or chair, and to end that day at nine, numb and sodden with weariness, after seventeen hours of physical labour.
===Chinese===
珀维斯·德容爱他年轻漂亮的妻子，她也爱他。可年轻的爱情要有色彩、温暖和美来滋养。倘若它一天从清晨四点开始——睡眼惺忪、迷迷糊糊地伸手，去摸从床柱或椅背上垂下来的那几件软塌塌的暗色衣裳；到晚上九点才告收工——熬过十七个钟头的力气活，浑身麻木、疲惫不堪——它便要变得平淡无味、讷讷无言。

===Original===
It was a wet summer. Pervus’s choice tomato plants, so carefully set out in the hope of a dry season, became draggled gray spectres in a waste of mire. Of fruit the field bore one tomato the size of a marble.
===Chinese===
那年夏天多雨。珀维斯精挑的番茄秧，原是指望个旱季才那样细心栽下的，如今在一片泥泞荒沼里成了灰扑扑、湿淋淋的鬼影。论果实，整块地结了一颗番茄，弹珠大小。

===Original===
For the rest, the crops were moderately successful on the DeJong place. But the work necessary to make this so was heartbreaking. Pervus and his hired helper, Jan Steen, used the hand sower and hand cultivator. It seemed to Selina that they were slaves to these buds, shoots, and roots that clamoured with a hundred thousand voices, “Let me out! Let me out!” She had known, during her winter at the Pools’, that Klaas, Roelf, and old Jakob worked early and late, but her months there had encompassed what is really the truck farmer’s leisure period. She had arrived in November. She had married in May. From May until October it was necessary to tend the fields with a concentration amounting to fury. Selina had never dreamed that human beings toiled like that for sustenance. Toil was a thing she had never encountered until coming to High Prairie. Now she saw her husband wrenching a living out of the earth by sheer muscle, sweat, and pain. During June, July, August, and September the good black prairie soil for miles around was teeming, a hotbed of plenty. There was born in Selina at this time a feeling for the land that she was never to lose. Perhaps the child within her had something to do with this. She was aware of a feeling of kinship with the earth; an illusion of splendour, of fulfilment. Sometimes, in a moment’s respite from her work about the house, she would stand in the kitchen doorway, her flushed face turned toward the fields. Wave on wave of green, wave on wave, until the waves melted into each other and became a verdant sea.
===Chinese===
除此之外，德容家地里的收成还算过得去。可要换来这一点收成，那份辛苦足以磨碎人心。珀维斯和他雇的帮工扬·斯汀（Jan Steen）用的是手摇播种机和手扶中耕器。在塞莉娜看来，他们成了这些花芽、嫩茎和根须的奴隶——那些东西十万条嗓门一齐嘶喊：「放我出去！放我出去！」在普尔家过冬那阵子，她原也知道克拉斯、鲁尔夫和老雅各布起早贪黑，可她住的那几个月，正赶上菜农一年里真正的闲季。她是十一月来的，五月成的婚。从五月到十月，照管田地须拿出一种近乎疯狂的专注。塞莉娜做梦也不曾想到，人为了糊口竟能这样劳苦。劳苦这东西，她来高草原之前从不曾照面。如今她眼看着丈夫凭着一身筋骨、汗水和疼痛，从土地里硬生生拧出一家生计。六月、七月、八月、九月，方圆多少英里膏腴的黑草原土生机勃勃，是一座丰饶的温床。就在此时，塞莉娜心里生出一种对土地的感情，此生再不曾失去。也许她腹中的孩子与此不无干系。她觉出一种与大地血肉相连的亲情；一种壮丽与圆满的幻觉。有时，忙里偷得片刻闲，她会站在厨房门口，绯红的脸转向田垄。绿浪一浪接一浪，一浪接一浪，直到浪与浪融为一体，化作一片青翠的海。

===Original===
As cabbages had been cabbages, and no more, to Klaas Pool, so, to Pervus, these carrots, beets, onions, turnips, and radishes were just so much produce, to be planted, tended, gathered, marketed. But to Selina, during that summer, they became a vital part in the vast mechanism of a living world. Pervus, earth, sun, rain, all elemental forces that laboured to produce the food for millions of humans. The sordid, grubby little acreage became a kingdom; the phlegmatic Dutch-American truck farmers of the region were high priests consecrated to the service of the divinity, Earth. She thought of Chicago’s children. If they had red cheeks, clear eyes, nimble brains it was because Pervus brought them the food that made them so. It was before the day when glib talk of irons, vitamines, arsenic entered into all discussion pertaining to food. Yet Selina sensed something of the meaning behind these toiling, patient figures, all unconscious of meaning, bent double in the fields for miles throughout High Prairie. Something of this she tried to convey to Pervus. He only stared, his blue eyes wide and unresponsive.
===Chinese===
当日卷心菜在克拉斯·普尔眼里不过是卷心菜，再无下文；如今胡萝卜、甜菜、洋葱、芜菁、小萝卜在珀维斯眼里也不过是些出产——种下、照料、收拔、上市，如此而已。可那个夏天，在塞莉娜眼里，它们成了活生生的世界这台庞大机器上的命脉一环。珀维斯，还有土地、太阳、雨水——全是劳作着为千百万人类生产口粮的元素之力。这块寒碜猥琐的小田产成了一座王国；本地那些木讷寡言的荷裔美国菜农，则是献身侍奉大地之神的高级祭司。她想起芝加哥的孩子们。他们若有红扑扑的脸蛋、清亮的眼睛、灵光的脑子，那是因为珀维斯把粮食送到了他们跟前，才养出这副模样。那时人们谈论吃食，还不到开口闭口铁质、维生素、砒霜的年代。然而塞莉娜隐隐觉出，这些遍布高草原方圆几里田间、弯腰曲背、劳苦坚忍的身影背后自有一番意义——他们自己却浑然不觉。这层意思，她试着讲给珀维斯听。他只是干瞪眼，蓝眼睛睁得老大，毫无回应。

===Original===
“Farm work grand! Farm work is slave work. Yesterday, from the load of carrots in town I didn’t make enough to bring you the goods for the child so when it comes you should have clothes for it. It’s better I feed them to the livestock.”
===Chinese===
「种地体面！种地是奴隶的活儿。昨儿一车胡萝卜拉进城，卖的钱还不够给你捎块料子回来，等孩子落了地，好歹有件衣裳穿。那些胡萝卜喂了牲口倒强些。」

===Original===
Pervus drove into the Chicago market every other day. During July and August he sometimes did not have his clothes off for a week. Together he and Jan Steen would load the wagon with the day’s garnering. At four he would start on the tedious trip into town. The historic old Haymarket on west Randolph Street had become the stand for market gardeners for miles around Chicago. Here they stationed their wagons in preparation for the next day’s selling. The wagons stood, close packed, in triple rows, down both sides of the curb and in the middle of the street. The early comer got the advantageous stand. There was no regular allotment of space. Pervus tried to reach the Haymarket by nine at night. Often bad roads made a detour necessary and he was late. That usually meant bad business next day. The men, for the most part, slept on their wagons, curled up on the wagon-seat or stretched out on the sacks. Their horses were stabled and fed in nearby sheds, with more actual comfort than the men themselves. One could get a room for twenty-five cents in one of the ramshackle rooming houses that faced the street. But the rooms were small, stuffy, none too clean; the beds little more comfortable than the wagons. Besides, twenty-five cents! You got twenty-five cents for half a barrel of tomatoes.
