You got twenty-five cents for a sack of potatoes. Onions brought seventy-five cents a sack. Cabbages went a hundred heads for two dollars, and they were five-pound heads. If you drove home with ten dollars in your pocket it represented a profit of exactly zero. The sum must go above that. No; one did not pay out twenty-five cents for the mere privilege of sleeping in a bed.
===Chinese===
珀维斯隔天就要赶一趟芝加哥的市场。七八月里，他有时一连一个礼拜衣裳都不离身。他和扬·斯汀两个把当天收拢的出产装上马车，四点钟便动身，踏上进城那段磨人的路。西伦道夫街上那座上了年头的老海马克特，早已成了芝加哥方圆多少里菜农们的市场。他们把车停在那儿，预备第二天发售。马车一辆贴一辆排成三行，沿着街道两侧的路缘排开，街心还有一排。到得早的占好位置。摊位没有定规的分配。珀维斯总想赶在夜里九点前抵达海马克特。路常常难走，绕个道便误了时辰。那多半意味着第二天生意不顺。这些人多半睡在自己的车上，蜷在车座上，或是直挺挺躺在麻袋上。他们的马倒安置在近旁的棚屋里，有吃有喝，比主人本人还受用些。花两毛五，就能在临街那些摇摇欲坠的寄宿公寓里赁一间屋。可那些屋子又小又闷，也说不上干净；床铺比车板舒服不了多少。再说，两毛五！半桶番茄才换两毛五。一袋土豆才换两毛五。洋葱一袋七毛五。卷心菜两块钱一百颗——还是五磅一颗的大菜头。要是赶车回家兜里揣着十块钱，利润恰好是个零。总得高过这个数才行。不；谁也不肯为睡一张床这点福分，白白掏出两毛五。

===Original===
One June day, a month or more after their marriage, Selina drove into Chicago with Pervus, an incongruous little figure in her bride’s finery perched on the seat of the vegetable wagon piled high with early garden stuff. They had started before four that afternoon, and reached the city at nine, though the roads were still heavy from the late May rains. It was, in a way, their wedding trip, for Selina had not been away from the farm since her marriage. The sun was bright and hot. Selina held an umbrella to shield herself from the heat and looked about her with enjoyment and interest. She chattered, turned her head this way and that, exclaimed, questioned. Sometimes she wished that Pervus would respond more quickly to her mood. A gay, volatile creature, she frisked about him like a friendly bright-eyed terrier about a stolid, ponderous St. Bernard.
===Chinese===
六月里的一天，成婚一个多月，塞莉娜随珀维斯赶车进了芝加哥——她一身新嫁娘的盛装，小小一个身影，高踞在堆满早春园蔬的菜车座上，同整车货物格格不入。那天下午不到四点他们就动身了，到城里已是九点——五月末的雨下过，路面还泥泞难行。这多少算是他们的新婚旅行，因为成婚以来塞莉娜还不曾离开过农庄。日头又亮又毒。塞莉娜撑一把伞遮阳，饶有兴味地四下张望。她说个不停，脑袋转来转去，一会儿惊叹，一会儿发问。有时她真盼珀维斯能更敏捷地应和她的心绪。这个快活、没长性的小东西，围着他蹦蹦跳跳，活像一只眼明心亮、亲亲热热的梗犬，绕着一头沉稳笨重的圣伯纳犬打转。

===Original===
As they jogged along now she revealed magnificent plans that had been forming in her imagination during the past four weeks. It had not taken her four weeks﻿—or days﻿—to discover that this great broad-shouldered man she had married was a kindly creature, tender and good, but lacking any vestige of initiative, of spirit. She marvelled, sometimes, at the memory of his boldness in bidding for her lunch box that evening of the raffle. It seemed incredible now, though he frequently referred to it, wagging his head doggishly and grinning the broadly complacent grin of the conquering male. But he was, after all, a dull fellow, and there was in Selina a dash of fire, of wholesome wickedness, of adventure, that he never quite understood. For her flashes of flame he had a mingled feeling of uneasiness and pride.
===Chinese===
这会儿，马车一路颠簸，她把过去四个礼拜在心里酝酿成的一幅幅宏伟蓝图，一一摊了出来。用不着四个礼拜——连四天也用不着——她便已看清：她嫁的这个宽肩膀的大个子男人，心肠不坏，温存和善，只是闯劲、精气神，一样都没有。有时她想起竞卖晚会那晚他出价抢她午餐盒的壮举，只觉得不可思议。如今回想更像天方夜谭，尽管他自己还三天两头提起，像狗似地晃着脑袋，咧开得胜的男人那副心宽气傲的大笑。可他到底是个乏味的人，而塞莉娜身上有一团火气、一点无害的邪气、一股闯荡的劲头，他从来不曾真懂。她那火光一闪的时候，他心里又不安、又得意，两样搅作一处。

===Original===
In the manner of all young brides, Selina started bravely out to make her husband over. He was handsome, strong, gentle; slow, conservative, morose. She would make him keen, daring, successful, buoyant. Now, bumping down the Halsted road, she sketched some of her plans in large dashing strokes.
===Chinese===
照着天下一切年轻新娘的做派，塞莉娜雄赳赳地动手改造丈夫。他英俊、结实、温存；也迟钝、守旧、阴沉。她要把他改得敏锐、果敢、发达、意气风发。此刻，马车顺着哈尔斯特德路一路颠簸，她大笔一挥，先给几项计划勾出个粗略的轮廓。

===Original===
“Pervus, we must paint the house in October, before the frost sets in, and after the summer work is over. White would be nice, with green trimmings. Though perhaps white isn’t practical. Or maybe green with darker green trimmings. A lovely background for the hollyhocks.” (Those that she and Roelf had planted showed no signs of coming up.) “Then that west sixteen. We’ll drain it.”
===Chinese===
「珀维斯，咱们十月里得把房子刷一遍漆，赶在上冻之前、夏天的活计忙完之后。白的体面，镶绿边。不过白的兴许不实用。要不就绿的，镶深绿边。正好衬那一片蜀葵。」（她和鲁尔夫种下的那些，至今不见出芽的动静。）「还有西边那十六英亩地。咱们得排水。」

===Original===
“Yeh, drain,” Pervus muttered. “It’s clay land. Drain and you have got yet clay. Hard clay soil.”
===Chinese===
「排，排吧，」珀维斯咕哝道。「那是黏土地。排了水，剩下的还是黏土。死硬的黏土。」

===Original===
Selina had the answer to that. “I know it. You’ve got to use tile drainage. And﻿—wait a minute﻿—humus. I know what humus is. It’s decayed vegetables. There’s always a pile by the side of the barn; and you’ve been using it on the quick land. All the west sixteen isn’t clay. Part of it’s muckland. All it needs is draining and manure. With potash, too, and phosphoric acid.”
===Chinese===
对这一套塞莉娜早有答案。「我知道。得用瓦管排水。还有——慢着——腐殖土。我知道腐殖土是什么。就是烂掉的菜。谷仓边上不是常年堆着一堆么；你一直把它上在早熟的松地里。西边那十六英亩也不全是黏土。有一部分是洼塘地。只消排水，再上粪肥。还得加钾肥，再加磷酸。」

===Original===
Pervus laughed a great hearty laugh that Selina found surprisingly infuriating. He put one great brown hand patronizingly on her flushed cheek; pinched it gently.
===Chinese===
珀维斯哈哈大笑，笑得那么酣畅，倒惹得塞莉娜出奇地光火。他伸出一只棕色大手，居高临下地按在她绯红的脸颊上，轻轻捏了一把。

===Original===
“Don’t!” said Selina, and jerked her head away. It was the first time she had ever resented a caress from him.
===Chinese===
「别动！」塞莉娜说着，猛地把头甩开。这是她头一回嫌恶他的爱抚。

===Original===
Pervus laughed again. “Well, well, well! School teacher is a farmer now, huh? I bet even Widow Paarlenberg don’t know as much as my little farmer about”﻿—he exploded again﻿—“about this, now, potash and﻿—what kind of acid? Tell me, little Lina, from where did you learn all this about truck farming?”
===Chinese===
珀维斯又笑了。「哎哟，哎哟，哎哟！教书先生如今种地了，啊？我打赌，帕伦贝格寡妇懂的都赶不上我这位小农妇懂的——」他又乐炸了——「懂的这些个，钾肥啦，还有——什么酸来着？告诉我，小莉娜，种菜这一套你打哪儿学来的？」

===Original===
“Out of a book,” Selina said, almost snappishly. “I sent to Chicago for it.”
===Chinese===
「打一本书上学来的，」塞莉娜说，口气近乎抢白。「我写信到芝加哥买的。」

===Original===
“A book! A book!” He slapped his knee. “A vegetable farmer out of a book.”
===Chinese===
「一本书！一本书！」他一巴掌拍在膝盖上。「照着书本种菜的人。」

===Original===
“Why not! The man who wrote it knows more about vegetable farming than anybody in all High Prairie. He knows about new ways. You’re running the farm just the way your father ran it.”
===Chinese===
「为什么不能！写书的人懂的种菜学问，比整个高草原随便谁都多。他懂新法子。你种这农场，用的还是你爹当年的老一套。」

===Original===
“What was good enough for my father is good enough for me.”
===Chinese===
「我爹使着合用的，我使着也合用。」

===Original===
“It isn’t!” cried Selina, “It isn’t! The book says clay loam is all right for cabbages, peas, and beans. It tells you how. It tells you how!” She was like a frantic little fly darting and pricking him on to accelerate the stolid sluggishness of his slow plodding gait.
===Chinese===
「不合用！」塞莉娜喊道。「不合用！书上说了，黏壤土种卷心菜、豌豆、菜豆都使得。书教你怎么办。书教你怎么办！」她像一只发急的小飞虫，绕着他又扑又叮，要催快他那一副木然迟缓、慢吞吞的懒步子。

===Original===
Having begun, she plunged on. “We ought to have two horses to haul the wagon to market. It would save you hours of time that you could spend on the place. Two horses, and a new wagon, green and red, like Klaas Pool’s.”
===Chinese===
话既开了头，她便一发不可收。「咱们该置两匹马拉车进市场。能给你省下好几个钟头，好花在地里。两匹马，再配一辆新车，绿漆红字，跟克拉斯·普尔家的一样。」

===Original===
Pervus stared straight ahead down the road between his horse’s ears much as Klaas Pool had done so maddeningly on Selina’s first ride on the Halsted road. “Fine talk. Fine talk.”
===Chinese===
珀维斯直勾勾盯着前方，望着马耳朵中间那段路，活像塞莉娜头一回坐车走哈尔斯特德路时，克拉斯·普尔那副叫人冒火的做派。「说得好听。说得好听。」

===Original===
“It isn’t talk. It’s plans. You’ve got to plan.”
===Chinese===
「这不是好听的话。这是打算。人总得有个打算。」

===Original===
“Fine talk. Fine talk.”
===Chinese===
「说得好听。说得好听。」

===Original===
“Oh!” Selina beat her knee with an impotent fist.
===Chinese===
「哼！」塞莉娜用一只无可奈何的拳头直捶自己的膝盖。

===Original===
It was the nearest they had ever come to quarrelling. It would seem that Pervus had the best of the argument, for when two years had passed the west sixteen was still a boggy clay mass, and unprolific; and the old house stared out shabby and paintless, at the dense willows by the roadside.
===Chinese===
这是两人离吵架最近的一回。看来这场争执是珀维斯占了上风：因为两年过去，西边那十六英亩仍是一团泥泞的黏土疙瘩，寸产不出；老屋子也照旧寒碜地瞪着路边的密柳，周身不见一点漆。

===Original===
They slept that night in one of the twenty-five-cent rooming houses. Rather, Pervus slept. The woman lay awake, listening to the city noises that had become strange in her ears; staring out into the purple-black oblong that was the open window, until that oblong became gray. She wept a little, perhaps. But in the morning Pervus might have noted (if he had been a man given to noting) that the fine jaw-line was set as determinedly as ever with an angle that spelled inevitably paint, drainage, humus, potash, phosphoric acid, and a horse team.
===Chinese===
那晚，他们宿在其中一家两毛五的寄宿公寓里。说确切些，睡熟的是珀维斯。这做妻子的却醒着，听着在她耳中已变得陌生的城市喧嚣，望着敞开的窗那一方紫黑色的长方块，直到那长方块泛出灰色。她也许小小哭了一场。可第二天早晨，珀维斯原本或许看得出来（假使他是个惯于留心的人），那道漂亮的下颌线仍一如既往地绷得紧紧的，带着一个角度，一板一眼明明白白地写着：刷漆、排水、腐殖土、钾肥、磷酸，外加一驾双马。

===Original===
She rose before four with Pervus, glad to be out of the stuffy little room with its spotted and scaly green wall paper, its rickety bed and chair. They had a cup of coffee and a slice of bread in the eating house on the first floor. Selina waited while he tended the horse. The night-watchman had been paid another twenty-five cents for watching the wagonload through the night as it stood in a row with the hundreds of others in the Haymarket. It was scarcely dawn when the trading began. Selina, watching it from the wagon seat, thought that this was a ridiculously haphazard and perilous method of distributing the food for whose fruition Pervus had toiled with aching back and tired arms. But she said nothing.
===Chinese===
还不到四点，她便随珀维斯起了身，乐得离开那间闷罐似的小屋——绿壁纸斑驳起皮，床和椅子都摇摇欲坠。他们在楼下的吃食铺里喝了一杯咖啡，啃了一片面包。珀维斯去安顿马，塞莉娜在一旁等着。昨夜又付了两毛五给守夜人，托他守着那车菜在海马克特过了一夜——同成百上千辆车排成一列。天刚蒙蒙亮，交易就开始了。塞莉娜坐在车座上望着，心想：这般全凭运气、险象环生的法子，竟用来分发粮食——珀维斯累得腰酸臂疼，才换来这些收成。可她什么也没说。

===Original===
She kept, perforce, to the house that first year, and the second. Pervus declared that his woman should never work in the fields as did many of the High Prairie wives and daughters. Of ready cash there was almost none. Pervus was hard put to it to pay Jan Steen his monthly wage during May, June, July, and August, when he was employed on the DeJong place, though Steen got but a pittance, being known as a poor hand, and “dumb.” Selina learned much that first year, and the second, but she said little. She kept the house in order﻿—rough work, and endless﻿—and she managed, miraculously, to keep herself looking fresh and neat. She understood now Maartje Pool’s drab garments, harassed face, heavily swift feet, never at rest. The idea of flowers in bowls was abandoned by July. Had it not been for Roelf’s faithful tending, the flower beds themselves, planted with such hopes, would have perished for lack of care.
===Chinese===
头一年，还有第二年，她不得不同屋子厮守。珀维斯放话：他的女人绝不许下地干活，不像高草原许多人家，连媳妇闺女都得下地。现钱几乎一个没有。五月、六月、七月、八月，扬·斯汀在德容家帮工的那几个月，珀维斯连他的月钱都凑得艰难——虽说斯汀挣的不过仨瓜俩枣，出了名的熊手，外加「木」。头一年，还有第二年，塞莉娜学到许多，却说得很少。她把家料理得井井有条——粗活，没个完——还奇迹般地把自己拾掇得清爽齐整。如今她懂了玛尔切·普尔那身灰旧的衣裳、那张操碎了心的脸、那双沉甸甸却总在疾走的脚——一刻不得消停。到七月，碗里插花的念头也撂下了。若不是鲁尔夫悉心照看，那些满怀指望栽下的花床，早因无人照管荒死了。

===Original===
Roelf came often to the house. He found there a tranquillity and peace never known in the Pool place, with its hubbub and clatter. In order to make her house attractive Selina had actually rifled her precious little bank hoard﻿—the four hundred and ninety-seven dollars left her by her father. She still had one of the clear white diamonds. She kept it sewed in the hem of an old flannel petticoat. Once she had shown it to Pervus.
===Chinese===
鲁尔夫常到这屋里来。他在这里寻到一种普尔家从未有过的安宁与静谧——那个家成天价闹哄哄、锅盆乱响。为了把这屋子拾掇得像样，塞莉娜竟真的掏空了自己那点宝贝的小积蓄——父亲留下的四百九十七块钱。她还剩一颗清亮的白钻石。她把它缝在一条旧法兰绒衬裙的裙边里。有一回，她拿给珀维斯看过。

===Original===
“If I sell this maybe we could get enough money to drain and tile.”
===Chinese===
「我要是把它卖了，兴许凑得出排水铺瓦管的钱。」

===Original===
Pervus took the stone, weighed it in his great palm, blinked as he always did when discussing a subject of which he was ignorant. “How much could you get for it? Fifty dollars, maybe. Five hundred is what I would need.”
===Chinese===
珀维斯接过那颗石头，摊在大手掌里掂了掂，眨巴起眼睛——一碰到他不懂的事，他总这样眨眼。「这个能卖多少？五十块，兴许吧。我要的是五百。」

===Original===
“I’ve got that. I’ve got it in the bank!”
===Chinese===
「我有啊。我就存着呢——在银行里！」

===Original===
“Well, maybe next spring. Right now I got my hands full, and more.”
===Chinese===
「行吧，兴许开春再说。眼下我这双手已经腾不开了，还往外冒呢。」

===Original===
To Selina that seemed a shortsighted argument. But she was too newly married to stand her ground; too much in love; too ignorant still of farm conditions.
===Chinese===
在塞莉娜听来，这话未免短视。可她新婚未久，还硬不起腰杆；爱得正深；对农事的底细也还太隔膜。

===Original===
The can of white paint and the brush actually did materialize. For weeks it was dangerous to sit, lean, or tread upon any paintable thing in the DeJong farmhouse without eliciting a cry of warning from Selina. She would actually have tried her hand at the outside of the house with a quart can and three-inch brush if Pervus hadn’t intervened. She hemmed dimity curtains, made slipcovers for the hideous parlour sofa and the ugliest of the chairs. Subscribed for a magazine called *House and Garden*. Together she and Roelf used to pore over this fascinating periodical. Terraces, lily-pools, leaded casements, cretonne, fireplaces, yew trees, pergolas, fountains﻿—they absorbed them all, exclaimed, admired, actually criticized. Selina was torn between an English cottage with timbered porch, bay window, stone flagging, and an Italian villa with a broad terrace on which she would stand in trailing white with a Russian wolfhound. If High Prairie had ever overheard one of these conversations between the farm woman who would always be a girl and the farm boy who had never been quite a child, it would have raised palms high in an “*Og heden!*” of horror. But High Prairie never heard, and wouldn’t have understood if it had. She did another strange thing: She placed the fine hand-carved oak chest Roelf had given her in a position so that her child should see it as soon as he opened his eyes in the morning. It was the most beautiful thing she possessed. She had, too, an incomplete set of old Dutch luster ware. It had belonged to Pervus’s mother, and to her mother before her. On Sunday nights Selina used this set for supper, though Pervus protested. And she always insisted that Dirk drink his milk out of one of the lovely jewel-like cups. Pervus thought this a piece of madness.
===Chinese===
那一罐白漆和那把刷子，倒真置办来了。好几个礼拜里，在德容家的农舍，随便往哪件能刷之物上坐一坐、靠一靠、踩一踩，都免不了招来塞莉娜一声警告。若不是珀维斯拦着，她真能提着一夸脱的小漆罐、一柄三寸宽的刷子，试着给房子外墙也上一手。她给麻纱窗帘缝边，给客厅那张丑得吓人的沙发做套子，给最丑的几把椅子做椅套。订了一份名为《住宅与花园》（*House and Garden*）的杂志。她和鲁尔夫常常一起趴在这本迷人的刊物上一页页细看。露台、百合水池、铅条格窗、印花装饰布、壁炉、紫杉、藤架、喷泉——他们照单全收，啧啧惊叹，叹赏，居然还敢批评两句。塞莉娜左也为难，右也为难：一忽儿想要一座英式村舍，木构门廊、凸窗、石板铺地；一忽儿想要一座意大利别墅，带一片宽阔的露台，她好穿一身曳地的白裙立在台上，牵一条俄国猎狼犬。高草原若偷听到这些谈话——一个永远是姑娘的农妇和一个从来不大像个孩子的农家少年之间的谈话——准会高举双掌，惊骇欲绝地大叫一声「*Og heden!*（荷兰语：哎呀天哪！）」。可高草原从不曾听见；即便听见了，也不会懂。她还做了一桩怪事：她把鲁尔夫送她的那只雕工精美的橡木箱摆在一个位置上，好教孩子清晨一睁眼就能望见它。这是她拥有的最美的东西。她还收着一整套不全的老荷兰虹彩陶器，是珀维斯的母亲传下来的，再往上是他外祖母的。礼拜天晚上，塞莉娜便用这套陶器开晚饭，珀维斯还直嘟囔。她又总是执意要德克用那只可爱的、宝石似的杯子喝牛奶。珀维斯认为这简直是疯病发作。

===Original===
Selina was up daily at four. Dressing was a swift and mechanical covering of the body. Breakfast must be ready for Pervus and Jan when they came in from the barn. The house to clean, the chickens to tend, sewing, washing, ironing, cooking. She contrived ways of minimizing her steps, of lightening her labour. And she saw clearly how the little farm was mismanaged through lack of foresight, imagination, and﻿—she faced it squarely﻿—through stupidity. She was fond of this great, kindly, blundering, stubborn boy who was her husband. But she saw him with amazing clearness through the mists of her love. There was something prophetic about the way she began to absorb knowledge of the farm work, of vegetable culture, of marketing. Listening, seeing, she learned about soil, planting, weather, selling. The daily talk of the house and fields was of nothing else. About this little twenty-five-acre garden patch there was nothing of the majesty of the Iowa, Illinois, and Kansas grain farms, with their endless billows of wheat and corn, rye, alfalfa, and barley rolling away to the horizon. Everything was done in diminutive here. An acre of this. Two acres of that. A score of chickens. One cow. One horse. Two pigs. Here was all the drudgery of farm life with none of its bounteousness, fine sweep, or splendour. Selina sensed that every inch of soil should have been made to yield to the utmost. Yet there lay the west sixteen, useless during most of the year; reliable never. And there was no money to drain it or enrich it; no ready cash for the purchase of profitable neighbouring acreage. She did not know the term intensive farming, but this was what she meant. Artificial protection against the treacherous climate of the Great Lakes region was pitifully lacking in Pervus’s plans. Now it would be hot with the humid, withering, sticky heat of the district. The ground was teeming, smoking, and the green things seemed actually to be pushing their way out of the earth so that one could almost see them growing, as in some absurd optical illusion. Then, without warning, would come the icy Lake Michigan wind, nipping the tender shoots with fiendish fingers. There should have been hotbeds and coldframes, forcing-hills, hand-boxes. There were almost none.
===Chinese===
塞莉娜天天四点起身。穿衣裳是一套麻利而机械的裹身动作。珀维斯和扬从牲口棚回来时，早饭必须现成。屋子要扫，鸡要喂，缝补、浆洗、熨烫、做饭。她想方设法省脚步、省力气。而她也看得明明白白，这座小农场是怎么办糟的——败在没有远见，没有想象，还败在——她敢于正视——愚蠢上。她喜欢这个做她丈夫的、高大、和善、毛手毛脚、死心眼的大孩子。可她隔着爱的云雾，把他看得透亮得出奇。她开始一点点吸收庄稼活、种菜、卖菜的门道，那样子颇有点先知的意味。听着，看着，她学会了土性、栽种、天时、行情。屋里田头，日日谈的无非这些。这二十五英亩的小菜园，全无艾奥瓦、伊利诺伊、堪萨斯那些粮食农场的气象——那边小麦玉米、黑麦苜蓿、大麦的浪头一浪接一浪，直滚到天边。这里样样都是小打小闹。这一样一英亩，那一样两英亩。二十来只鸡。一头牛。一匹马。两头猪。庄稼活的苦，这里一样不缺；庄稼活的丰饶、气派、壮丽，这里一样没有。塞莉娜觉出，这里的每一寸土都该榨到极致才对。可西边那十六英亩还撂在那里，一年里大半时候百无一用；从没个靠得住的时候。又没有钱排水、肥田；也没有现钱买下左近能生利的地亩。集约耕作这个词她还不知道，可她想的就是这个。珀维斯的盘算里，对付五大湖地区反复无常的气候的人工防护，少得可怜。天一时热起来，热的是本地区那种又潮、又灼、又黏的热。地里热气腾腾，咕嘟冒烟，那些绿油油的东西仿佛真在往外拱，人几乎看得见它们生长，像什么荒唐的视错觉。跟着，说来就来，密歇根湖的冰风到了，恶魔似的手指掐死嫩芽。本该有温床冷床、催熟土垄、活动罩箱。几乎一样没有。

===Original===
These things Selina saw, but not quite clearly. She went about her housework, now dreamily, now happily. Her physical condition swayed her mood. Sometimes, in the early autumn, when the days became cooler, she would go to where Pervus and Jan were working in the fields in the late afternoon gathering the produce for that night’s trip to market. She would stand there, a bit of sewing in her hand, perhaps, the wind ruffling her hair, whipping her skirts, her face no longer pale, tilted a little toward the good sun like a lovely tawny flower. Sometimes she sat perched on a pile of empty sacks, or on an upended crate, her sewing in her hand. She was happiest at such times﻿—most content﻿—except for the pang she felt at sight of the great dark splotch on the blue of Pervus’s work-shirt where the sweat stained it.
===Chinese===
这些事塞莉娜都看在眼里，只是还看不十分真切。她操持着家务，一忽儿如梦，一忽儿欢喜。身子的情况左右着她的心绪。有时，初秋天气转凉，傍晚她便走到珀维斯和扬干活的田里去——他们正收拢当夜的出产，预备进城赶集。她就站在那里，手里兴许拿着点针线，风撩着她的头发，掀着她的裙角，那张不再苍白的脸微微仰向那轮好太阳，像一朵可爱的黄褐色的花。有时她高踞在一堆空麻袋上，或是一只倒扣的条板箱上，针线活儿拿在手里。这样的时刻她最快活——最知足——只除了一桩扎心的事：一眼瞥见珀维斯的蓝布工装衫上，叫汗浸出的那一大片深色汗渍。

===Original===
She had come out so one autumn afternoon. She was feeling particularly gay, buoyant. In one of his rare hours of leisure Roelf Pool had come to help her with her peony roots which Pervus had brought her from Chicago for fall planting. Roelf had dug the trench, deep and wide, mulched it with cow-manure, banked it. They were to form a double row up the path to the front of the house, and in her mind’s eye Selina already saw them blooming when spring should come, shaggy balls of luscious pink. Now Roelf was lending a hand to Pervus and Jan as they bent over the late beets and radishes. It was a day all gold and blue and scarlet; warm for the season with a ripe mellow warmth like yellow chartreuse. There were stretches of seal-black loam where the vegetables had been uprooted. Bunches of them, string-tied, lay ready for gathering into baskets. Selina’s eye was gladdened by the clear coral of radishes flung against the rich black loam.
===Chinese===
一天秋日午后，她就是这样走出来的。她那天格外轻快，兴头十足。鲁尔夫·普尔难得偷得半日闲，过来帮她种芍药根——那是珀维斯从芝加哥捎回来给她秋栽的。鲁尔夫把沟挖得又深又宽，垫上牛粪，又培起土埂。芍药要顺着通往前门的小路栽成两行，塞莉娜心里的眼睛已经望见来年春天它们开花的模样——一个个毛茸茸、水灵灵的粉球。这会儿鲁尔夫又搭手帮珀维斯和扬收晚茬的甜菜和小萝卜。那一天，金一片，蓝一片，猩红一片；时令里难得的暖，熟透醇和的暖，像黄色查特酒（chartreuse）。起了菜的地里，露出一片片海豹黑的黑土。一捆捆菜用草绳扎着，码放停当，等着装筐。小萝卜那明净的珊瑚红撒在油黑肥沃的黑土上，看得塞莉娜眼里发亮。

===Original===
“A jewel, Pervus!” she cried. “A jewel in an Ethiop’s ear!”
===Chinese===
「一颗宝石，珀维斯！」她喊道。「黑姑娘耳朵上的一颗宝石！」

===Original===
“What?” said Pervus, looking up, amiable but uncomprehending. But the boy smiled. Selina had left him that book for his own when she went away. Suddenly Selina stooped and picked up one of the scarlet and green clusters tied with its bit of string. Laughing, she whipped out a hairpin and fastened the bunch in her hair just behind her ear. An absurd thing to do, and childish. It should have looked as absurd as it was, but it didn’t. Instead it was like a great crimson flower there. Her cheeks were flushed with the hot sun. Her fine dark hair was windblown and a little loosened, her dress open at the throat. Her figure was fuller, her breast had a richer curve, for the child was four months on the way. She was laughing. At a little exclamation from Roelf, Pervus looked up, as did Jan. Selina took a slow rhythmic step, and another, her arms upraised, a provocative lovely bacchic little figure there in the fields under the hot blue sky. Jan Steen wiped the sweat from his brown face, a glow in his eyes.
===Chinese===
「什么？」珀维斯抬起头说，和颜悦色，却全然不懂。男孩却笑了。塞莉娜当年离开时，把那本书留给他做了私藏。忽然，塞莉娜弯腰拾起一小捆红绿相间、还拴着草绳的萝卜。她笑着拔下一根发针，把那捆花别在耳后的头发上。这是件荒唐事，孩子气。照理它该显得有多荒唐就多荒唐，可偏不。它倒像那儿开出的一朵大红花。她的双颊叫烈日晒得绯红。她那头细密的黑发被风吹乱，微微松了，领口敞着。她的身量丰满了，胸脯的弧线更饱满了，因为孩子已有了四个月。她笑着。鲁尔夫低呼了一声，珀维斯抬起头，扬也抬起头。塞莉娜踏出一个缓慢而带节奏的舞步，又一步，双臂高举——烈日蓝天下，田野里一个撩人的、可爱的、酒神般的小小人影。扬·斯汀抹去棕色脸上的汗水，眼里放着光。

===Original===
“You are like the calendar!” cried Roelf, “on the wall in the parlour.” A cheap but vivid and not unlovely picture of a girl with cherries in her hair. It hung in the Pool farmhouse.
===Chinese===
「你活像那张日历！」鲁尔夫喊道，「客厅墙上挂的那张。」那是一张俗气却鲜艳、并不讨嫌的画儿：一个头发上缀樱桃的姑娘。它就挂在普尔家的农舍里。

===Original===
Pervus DeJong showed one of his rare storms of passion. Selina had not seen that blaze of blue in his eyes since the night, months ago, in the Pools’ kitchen. But that blaze had been a hot and burning blue, like the sky of today. This was a bitter blue, a chill and freezing thing, like the steel-blue of ice in the sun.
===Chinese===
珀维斯·德容发作了一阵他难得一见的雷霆。自打几个月前普尔家厨房那一夜，塞莉娜再没见过他眼里那道蓝火焰。可那一夜的蓝是灼热的、燃烧的蓝，像今天的天空。这一道却是苦涩的蓝，一种冰冷刺骨的东西，像太阳底下寒冰的钢蓝。

===Original===
“Take them things out of your hair now! Take shame to yourself!” He strode over to her and snatched the things from her hair and threw them down and ground them into the soft earth with his heavy heel. A long coil of her fine dark hair came rippling over her shoulder as he did so. She stood looking at him, her eyes wide, dark, enormous in her face now suddenly white.
===Chinese===
「快把你头发里那些玩意儿摘下来！你自己也不害臊！」他大步冲到她跟前，一把从她头发里扯下那些东西，摔在地上，用沉重的鞋跟碾进软土里。他扯的时候，她一头细密的黑发有一长绺波浪似的泻下肩来。她站着望他，眼睛睁得老大、乌黑，在骤然变白的那张脸上显得巨大。

===Original===
His wrath was born of the narrow insular mind that fears gossip. He knew that the hired man would tell through the length and width of High Prairie how Pervus DeJong’s wife pinned red radishes in her hair and danced in the fields like a loose woman.
===Chinese===
他的怒火，出自那种惧怕闲话的狭隘心眼。他知道那个雇工会把这事传遍高草原的沟沟坎坎：珀维斯·德容的老婆往头发上别红萝卜，还在地里跳舞，活像个不正经的女人。

===Original===
Selina had turned, fled to the house. It was their first serious quarrel. For days she was hurt, ashamed, moody. They made it up, of course. Pervus was contrite, abject almost. But something that belonged to her girlhood had left her that day.
===Chinese===
塞莉娜转身逃回了屋。这是他们头一回正经的争吵。一连几天，她委屈、羞愧、郁郁寡欢。当然，两人后来和好了。珀维斯悔了，几乎低声下气。可有一样属于她少女时代的东西，那一天离开了她。

===Original===
During that winter she was often hideously lonely. She never got over her hunger for companionship. Here she was, a gregarious and fun-loving creature, buried in a snowbound Illinois prairie farmhouse with a husband who looked upon conversation as a convenience, not a pastime. She learned much that winter about the utter sordidness of farm life. She rarely saw the Pools; she rarely saw anyone outside her own little household. The front room﻿—the parlour﻿—was usually bitterly cold but sometimes she used to slip in there, a shawl over her shoulders, and sit at the frosty window to watch for a wagon to go by, or a chance pedestrian up the road. She did not pity herself, nor regret her step. She felt, physically, pretty well for a childbearing woman; and Pervus was tender, kindly, sympathetic, if not always understanding. She struggled gallantly to keep up the small decencies of existence. She loved the glow in Pervus’s eyes when she appeared with a bright ribbon, a fresh collar, though he said nothing and perhaps she only fancied that he noticed. Once or twice she had walked the mile and a half of slippery road to the Pools’, and had sat in Maartje’s warm bright bustling kitchen for comfort. It seemed to her incredible that a little more than a year ago she had first stepped into this kitchen in her modish brown lady’s-cloth dress, muffled in wraps, cold but elated, interested, ready for adventure, surprise, discomfort﻿—anything. And now here she was in that same kitchen, amazingly, unbelievably Mrs. Pervus DeJong, truck farmer’s wife, with a child soon to be born. And where was adventure now? And where was life? And where the love of chance bred in her by her father?
===Chinese===
那年冬天，她常常孤单得怕人。她对有人作伴的渴念，从来不曾消解。她天生爱群处、爱热闹，如今却被埋在伊利诺伊州一座大雪封门的草原农舍里，守着一个把谈话当实用、不当消遣的丈夫。那个冬天，她深深领教了农家生涯彻头彻尾的寒碜。她难得见普尔家的人；自家这个小圈子以外，难得见任何人。前屋——客厅——通常冷得刺骨，可她有时会披一条披肩溜进去，坐在结了霜花的窗前，盼一辆马车驶过，或路上偶然闪出一个行人。她不自怜，也不后悔走的这一步。就身子骨说，她一个怀着身孕的女人，自觉还相当不坏；珀维斯也温存、和气、体恤，虽说未必总是体谅。她咬着牙苦苦撑持，要保住生活里那一点点体面。她系一条鲜亮的缎带、换一副干净的领子走到跟前，珀维斯眼里会放光——她爱看那光，虽说他不吭一声，也许只是她自以为他留意了。有一两回，她踏着一里半溜滑的路走到普尔家，在玛尔切那温暖、亮堂、忙忙碌碌的厨房里坐一坐，讨一点暖。想想真难以置信：不过一年多以前，她才头一回踏进这间厨房——一身时髦的棕色呢面小姐裙，裹着围巾，冻得发僵却兴致高昂，好奇心盛，随时准备迎候冒险、惊异、苦头——什么都行。如今她竟坐在这同一间厨房里，不可思议地、难以置信地成了珀维斯·德容太太，一个菜农的妻子，肚里的孩子眼看要落生。可如今冒险在哪儿？生活在哪儿？父亲种进她心里的那份对机运的爱恋，又到哪儿去了？

===Original===
The two years following Dirk’s birth were always somewhat vague in Selina’s mind, like a dream in which horror and happiness are inextricably blended. The boy was a plump hardy infant who employed himself cheerfully in whatever spot Selina happened to deposit him. He had his father’s blond exterior, his mother’s brunette vivacity. At two he was a child of average intelligence, sturdy physique, and marked good humour. He almost never cried.
===Chinese===
德克出生后的那两年，在塞莉娜记忆里始终有点模糊，像一场恐怖与幸福纠缠难分的梦。这男孩是个胖乎乎、结结实实的小娃娃，塞莉娜把他搁在哪儿，他就在哪儿自得其乐。他外表随了父亲的金发白肤，那股活泼劲儿却随了母亲的黑发血脉。两岁上，他是个智力平平、体格壮实、脾气出奇地好的孩子。他几乎从来不哭。

===Original===
He was just twelve months old when Selina’s second child﻿—a girl﻿—was born dead. Twice during those two years Pervus fell victim to his so-called rheumatic attacks following the early spring planting when he was often forced to stand in water up to his ankles. He suffered intensely and during his illness was as tractable as a goaded bull. Selina understood why half of High Prairie was bent and twisted with rheumatism﻿—why the little Dutch Reformed church on Sunday mornings resembled a shrine to which sick and crippled pilgrims creep.
===Chinese===
他刚满十二个月，塞莉娜的第二个孩子——一个女孩——生下来就是死胎。那两年里，珀维斯两次犯他那所谓风湿的毛病，都在早春播种之后——那阵子他常常不得不站进没踝的水里。他疼得死去活来，病中却乖顺得赛过一头被戳急了的公牛。塞莉娜于是明白了，高草原为什么有一半人叫风湿症折磨得弯腰驼背——那座小小的荷兰归正会教堂，礼拜天早晨为什么像座圣殿，害病的、瘸腿的香客都朝它爬去。

===Original===
High Prairie was kind to the harried household. The farm women sent Dutch dainties. The men lent a hand in the fields, though they were hard put to it to tend their own crops at this season. The Widow Paarlenberg’s neat smart rig was frequently to be seen waiting under the willows in the DeJong yard. The Paarlenberg, still widow, still Paarlenberg, brought soups and chickens and cakes which never stuck in Selina’s throat because she refused to touch them. The Widow Paarlenberg was what is known as good-hearted. She was happiest when someone else was in trouble. Hearing of an illness, a catastrophe, “*Og heden!*” she would cry, and rush off to the scene with sustaining soup. She was the sort of lady bountiful who likes to see her beneficiaries benefit before her very eyes. If she brought them soup at ten in the morning she wanted to see that soup consumed.
===Chinese===
高草原待这户焦头烂额的人家不薄。农妇们送来荷兰点心。男人们下地搭把手——虽说这个时节，他们自家的庄稼也够他们脚打后脑勺。帕伦贝格寡妇那辆整洁漂亮的轻便马车，常可以看见停在德容家场院的柳树底下。帕伦贝格家这位——寡妇照旧是寡妇，帕伦贝格照旧姓帕伦贝格——送来汤水、鸡和糕饼；这些东西从不曾噎在塞莉娜喉咙里，因为她压根不肯沾。帕伦贝格寡妇是那种出了名的心肠好的人。别人遭了难，她最快活。一听说谁病了、谁家出了事，她便喊一声「*Og heden!*（荷兰语：哎呀天哪！）」，提着一罐滋补的汤火速赶赴现场。她是那种女施主，最爱亲眼看着受她恩惠的人当着她的面受惠。早晨十点送去的汤，她就要亲眼看着那汤喝下去。

===Original===
“Eat it all,” she would urge. “Take it now, while it is hot. See, you are looking better already. Just another spoonful.”
===Chinese===
「都吃了，」她总这样劝。「趁热，现在就吃。瞧，你气色已经好些了。再来一勺。」

===Original===
In the DeJongs’ plight she found a grisly satisfaction, cloaked by commiseration. Selina, white and weak following her tragic second confinement, still found strength to refuse the widow’s sustaining potions. The widow, her silks making a gentle susurrus in the bare little bedroom, regarded Selina with eyes in which pity and triumph made horrid conflict. Selina’s eyes, enormous now in her white face, were twin pools of Peake pride.
===Chinese===
德容家这场劫难里，她寻得了一丝阴惨惨的快意，外头罩着怜悯的幌子。塞莉娜经过那场悲惨的二次分娩，苍白虚弱，却仍有气力回绝寡妇的滋补汤水。寡妇那一身绸衣窸窣作响，把这间空落落的小卧室轻轻拂动；她端详着塞莉娜，一双眼睛里，怜悯与得意正杀气腾腾地交战。塞莉娜的眼睛，在她苍白的脸上显得巨大，是两汪皮克家的傲气。

===Original===
“It’s most kind of you, Mrs. Paarlenberg, but I don’t like soup.”
===Chinese===
「您心眼儿真是太好了，帕伦贝格太太，可我不爱喝汤。」

===Original===
“A whole chicken boiled in it.”
===Chinese===
「一整只鸡炖在里头呢。」

===Original===
“Especially chicken soup. Neither does Pervus. But I’m sure Mrs. Voorhees will enjoy it.” This being Pervus’s old housekeeper pressed now into temporary emergency service.
===Chinese===
「尤其是鸡汤。珀维斯也不喝。不过我担保弗里斯太太一定爱喝。」这位弗里斯太太，就是珀维斯从前的看屋老太太，如今被拉来应急。

===Original===
It was easy to see why the DeJong house still was unpainted two years after Selina’s rosy plans began to form; why the fences still sagged, the wagon creaked, the single horse hauled the produce to market.
===Chinese===
这就难怪了：塞莉娜那些玫瑰色的计划起了两年，德容家的房子还是不见漆；难怪栅栏照旧歪着，马车照旧吱嘎响，还是独马拉着出产上市场。

===Original===
Selina had been married almost three years when there came to her a letter from Julie Hempel, now married. The letter had been sent to the Klaas Pool farm and Jozina had brought it to her. Though she had not seen it since her days at Miss Fister’s school, Selina recognized with a little hastening heartbeat the spidery handwriting with the shading and curleycues. Seated on her kitchen steps in her calico dress she read it.
===Chinese===
塞莉娜成婚快三年的时候，收到了朱莉·亨佩尔——如今也嫁了人——的一封信。信寄到了克拉斯·普尔家的农场，约西娜给她捎了来。虽打菲斯特女子学校的日子以后再没见过，塞莉娜还是一眼认出那笔蛛丝细字——又描粗细、又带花卷——心跳不由得快了些。她穿着印花布裙，坐在厨房台阶上读信。

===Original===
Darling Selina:﻿—

I thought it was so queer that you didn’t answer my letter and now I know you must have thought it queer that I did not answer yours. I found your letter to me, written long ago, when I was going over Mother’s things last week. It was the letter you must have written when I was in Kansas City. Mother had never given it to me. I am not reproaching her. You see, I had written you from Kansas City, but had sent my letter to Mamma to mail because I never could remember that funny address of yours in the country.

Mamma died three weeks ago. Last week I was going over her things﻿—a trying task, you may imagine﻿—and there were your two letters addressed to me. She had never destroyed them. Poor Mamma﻿ ﻿…

Well, dear Selina, I suppose you don’t even know that I am married. I married Michael Arnold of Kansas City. The Arnolds were in the packing business there, you know. Michael has gone into business with Pa here in Chicago and I suppose you have heard of Pa’s success. Just all of a sudden he began to make a great deal of money after he left the butcher business and went into the yards﻿—the stockyards, you know. Poor Mamma was so happy these last few years, and had everything that was beautiful. I have two children. Eugene and Pauline.

I am getting to be quite a society person. You would laugh to see me. I am on the Ladies’ Entertainment Committee of the World’s Fair. We are supposed to entertain all the visiting big bugs﻿—that is the lady bugs. There! How is that for a joke?

I suppose you know about the Infanta Eulalie. Of Spain, you know. And what she did about the Potter Palmer ball.﻿ ﻿…
===Chinese===
亲爱的塞莉娜：——

我还直纳闷，你怎么不回我的信；如今我可明白了，你准也觉得奇怪，我怎么不回你的。上礼拜我翻检妈妈的东西，发现了你很久以前写给我的那封信，准是我住在堪萨斯城那阵子你写的。妈妈一直没转交给我。我不是怪她。是这么回事：我从堪萨斯城给你写过信，可我把信交给妈妈代寄——你乡下那个古怪地址，我怎么也记不住。

妈妈三个礼拜前去世了。上礼拜我收拾她的遗物——你可想见，那是件熬人的差事——翻出了你写给我的两封信。她一直没有销毁。可怜的妈妈……

对了，亲爱的塞莉娜，我估摸你还不知道我嫁人了。我嫁了堪萨斯城的迈克尔·阿诺德。你知道，阿诺德家在那边做的是肉品加工生意。迈克尔如今到芝加哥跟爸合伙了，爸的发达你总该有所耳闻。他撂下肉铺的营生、进了场——就是牲畜围场，你知道——打那以后，忽然就大把大把地挣起钱来。可怜的妈妈这几年好福气，什么好东西都享上了。我有两个孩子，尤金（Eugene）和波琳（Pauline）。

我如今快混成交际场上的人物了。你见了我准得笑。我在世界博览会（World’s Fair）的女士招待委员会里当差。上头派咱们招待各方来访的大人物（big bugs）——也就是瓢虫太太们（lady bugs）。瞧！这话逗不逗？

欧拉利娅公主的事你总知道吧。西班牙的那位，你知道。还有她在波特·帕尔默舞会上那一手。……

===Original===
Selina, holding the letter in her work-stained hand, looked up and across the fields and away to where the prairie met the sky and closed in on her; her world. The Infanta Eulalie of Spain.﻿ ﻿… She went back to the letter.
===Chinese===
塞莉娜攥着信——那只手沾着干活的污痕——抬起眼来，望过田野，一直望到草原与天空相接、向她四面围拢过来的地方；那是她的世界。西班牙的欧拉利娅公主。……她低头接着读信。

===Original===
Well, she came to Chicago for the Fair and Mrs. Potter Palmer was to give a huge reception and ball for her. Mrs. P. is head of the whole committee, you know, and I must say she looks queenly with her white hair so beautifully dressed and her diamond dog-collar and her black velvet and all. Well, at the very last minute the Infanta refused to attend the ball because she had just heard that Mrs. P. was an innkeeper’s wife. Imagine! The Palmer House, of course.
===Chinese===
是这么回事：她来芝加哥参观世博会，波特·帕尔默太太（Mrs. Potter Palmer）要为她办一场盛大的招待舞会。帕太太是整个委员会的头儿，你知道，而且我得说，她那样子真有王后气派——一头白发梳得那样好看，戴着钻石项圈，一身黑丝绒，还有那一切的排场。谁知到了最后一分钟，公主忽然不肯赴会了，因为她刚听说，帕太太原来是个开客栈的媳妇。想想看！不就是帕尔默旅馆么。

===Original===
Selina, holding the letter in her hand, imagined.
===Chinese===
塞莉娜手里捏着信，当真想象了一番。

===Original===
It was in the third year of Selina’s marriage that she first went into the fields to work. Pervus had protested miserably, though the vegetables were spoiling in the ground.
===Chinese===
塞莉娜成婚的第三年，她头一回下地干活。虽说菜眼看烂在地里，珀维斯还是凄凄惶惶地不依。

===Original===
“Let them rot,” he said. “Better the stuff rots in the ground. DeJong women folks they never worked in the fields. Not even in Holland. Not my mother or my grandmother. It isn’t for women.”
===Chinese===
「让它们烂去吧，」他说。「东西烂在地里反倒干净。德容家的娘儿们从来不下地。在荷兰老家也不下。我妈不下，我奶奶也不下。那不是女人干的活。」

===Original===
Selina had regained health and vigour after two years of wretchedness. She felt steel-strong and even hopeful again, sure sign of physical well-being. Long before now she had realized that this time must inevitably come. So she answered briskly, “Nonsense, Pervus. Working in the field’s no harder than washing or ironing or scrubbing or standing over a hot stove in August. Women’s work! Housework’s the hardest work in the world. That’s why men won’t do it.”
===Chinese===
熬过两年苦日子，塞莉娜又养回了气力和精神。她觉得自己硬得像钢，甚至又有了盼头——这是身子康泰的铁证。她早就明白，这一天早晚要来。于是她干脆利落地答道：「胡说，珀维斯。下地不比洗衣、熨衣、擦地更累，也不比八月里守着烧烫的炉灶更熬人。女人的活计！天底下顶重的活就是家务。要不男人怎么死活不肯沾呢。」

===Original===
She would often take the boy Dirk with her into the fields, placing him on a heap of empty sacks in the shade. He invariably crawled off this lowly throne to dig and burrow in the warm black dirt. He even made as though to help his mother, pulling at the rooted things with futile fingers, and sitting back with a bump when a shallow root did unexpectedly yield to his tugging.
===Chinese===
她常常把小德克带去地里，把他安顿在阴凉处一堆空麻袋上。他回回都要从这座寒酸的王座上爬下来，钻进温热的黑土里又挖又拱。他甚至装模作样帮母亲干活，伸着不顶事的手指去拽那些扎了根的东西；偶尔哪条浅根出其不意地松了劲，他便扑通坐个屁股墩。

===Original===
“Look! He’s a farmer already,” Pervus would say.
===Chinese===
「瞧！他已经是个庄稼汉了。」珀维斯总这么说。

===Original===
But within Selina something would cry, “No! No!”
===Chinese===
可塞莉娜心里总有什么在喊：「不！不！」

===Original===
During May, June, and July Pervus worked not only from morning until night, but by moonlight as well, and Selina worked with him. Often their sleep was a matter of three hours only, or four.
===Chinese===
五月、六月、七月，珀维斯不但从早干到黑，月亮底下也干，塞莉娜陪着一起干。他们的觉常常只睡得上三个钟头，或者四个。

===Original===
So two years went﻿—three years﻿—four. In the fourth year of Selina’s marriage she suffered the loss of her one woman friend in all High Prairie. Maartje Pool died in childbirth, as was so often the case in this region where a Gampish midwife acted as obstetrician. The child, too, had not lived. Death had not been kind to Maartje Pool. It had brought neither peace nor youth to her face, as it so often does. Selina, looking down at the strangely still figure that had been so active, so bustling, realized that for the first time in the years she had known her she was seeing Maartje Pool at rest. It seemed incredible that she could lie there, the infant in her arms, while the house was filled with people and there were chairs to be handed, space to be cleared, food to be cooked and served. Sitting there with the other High Prairie women Selina had a hideous feeling that Maartje would suddenly rise up and take things in charge; rub and scratch with capable fingers the spatters of dried mud on Klaas Pool’s black trousers (he had been in the yard to see to the horses); quiet the loud wailing of Geertje and Jozina; pass her gnarled hand over Roelf’s wide-staring tearless eyes; wipe the film of dust from the parlour table that had never known a speck during her regime.
===Chinese===
就这样过了两年——三年——四年。塞莉娜成婚的第四年，她失去了在高草原唯一的一位女性朋友。玛尔切·普尔死于难产——本地常常如此：充当产科医生的，是一位甘普式（Gampish，狄更斯笔下那个出了名的接生婆）的接生婆。孩子也没能活。死亡对玛尔切·普尔并不仁慈。它不曾像惯常那样，给她的脸带来安宁或年轻。塞莉娜俯望着那个曾经那样麻利、那样风风火火的人儿，如今离奇地静止着，这才意识到：认识她这些年，自己是头一回看见玛尔切·普尔歇着。屋里满是人，椅子要递，地方要腾，饭菜要做要端——她竟然还能那样躺着，怀里抱着婴儿，简直叫人不敢相信。塞莉娜同高草原其余的妇人们坐在那里，心里有个可怕的念头挥之不去：玛尔切随时会突然坐起来，一应接管；用她那双能干的手指，把克拉斯·普尔黑裤子上的泥点子搓掉、抠净（他刚在场院里照看马来）；把海尔切和约西娜嚎啕的哭声压下去；用她那节瘤纵横的手掌，在鲁尔夫那双瞪得老大、无泪的眼上抚一抚；再把客厅桌上那层浮尘擦掉——她当家的那些年，那桌上从来不曾落过一个斑点。

===Original===
“You can’t run far enough,” Maartje had said. “Except you stop living you can’t run away from life.”
===Chinese===
「你逃不出多远，」玛尔切生前说过。「除非你不活了，谁也没法从生活里逃出去。」

===Original===
Well, she had run far enough this time.
===Chinese===
唔，这一回，她逃得够远了。

===Original===
Roelf was sixteen now, Geertje twelve, Jozina eleven. What would this household do now, Selina wondered, without the woman who had been so faithful a slave to it? Who would keep the pigtails﻿—no longer giggling﻿—in clean ginghams and decent square-toed shoes? Who, when Klaas broke out in rumbling Dutch wrath against what he termed Roelf’s “dumb” ways, would say, “*Og*, Pool, leave the boy alone once. He does nothing.” Who would keep Klaas himself in order; cook his meals, wash his clothes, iron his shirts, take pride in the great ruddy childlike giant?
===Chinese===
鲁尔夫如今十六了，海尔切十二，约西娜十一。塞莉娜不禁想：这个家往后可怎么办，少了那个像忠心奴隶一样伺候了它一辈子的女人？往后，谁给那几条不再咯咯傻笑的小辫子换干净的方格布裙、穿像样的方头鞋？往后，克拉斯再轰隆隆发作荷兰脾气、数落鲁尔夫那套「木」性子的时候，谁来劝一句：「*Og*（荷兰语：哎呀），普尔，就饶这孩子一回吧。他没干什么。」往后，谁伺候克拉斯本人；给他做饭、洗衣裳、熨衬衫，心疼这个红润天真、孩子似的大个子巨人？

===Original===
Klaas answered these questions just nine months later by marrying the Widow Paarlenberg. High Prairie was rocked with surprise. For months this marriage was the talk of the district. They had gone to Niagara Falls on a wedding trip; Pool’s place was going to have this improvement and that; no, they were going to move to the Widow Paarlenberg’s large farmhouse (they would always call her that); no, Pool was putting in a bathroom with a bathtub and running water; no, they were going to buy the Stikker place between Pool’s and Paarlenberg’s and make one farm of it, the largest in all High Prairie, Low Prairie, or New Haarlem. Well, no fool like an old fool.
===Chinese===
这些问题，克拉斯只用九个月便作了回答：他娶了帕伦贝格寡妇。高草原整个被惊得晃了三晃。一连几个月，这门亲事都是全区的话柄。听说他们去尼亚加拉瀑布（Niagara Falls）度了蜜月；听说普尔家的庄子要添这个添那个；不对，是说要搬进帕伦贝格寡妇家的大农宅（这称呼，人们是一辈子也改不了口了）；也不对，是说普尔家装了浴室，带浴缸和自来水；再不对，是说他们要把普尔家和帕伦贝格家之间那块斯蒂克（Stikker）家的地买下来，并成一座农场——高草原、低草原带新哈勒姆最大的一座。唉，老糊涂才是天下第一号的糊涂。

===Original===
So insatiable was High Prairie’s curiosity that every scrap of fresh news was swallowed at a gulp. When the word went round of Roelf’s flight from the farm, no one knew where, it served only as sauce to the great dish of gossip.
===Chinese===
高草原的好奇心没有个餍足，每一星半点新鲜消息，都叫它一口囫囵吞下。待到鲁尔夫弃家出走的消息传开——没人知道他去了哪里——那也不过给这盆闲话大菜添一勺浇汁。

===Original===
Selina had known. Pervus was away at the market when Roelf had knocked at the farmhouse door one night at eight, had turned the knob and entered, as usual. But there was nothing of the usual about his appearance. He wore his best suit﻿—his first suit of store clothes, bought at the time of his mother’s funeral. It never had fitted him; now was grotesquely small for him. He had shot up amazingly in the last eight or nine months. Yet there was nothing of the ridiculous about him as he stood before her now, tall, lean, dark. He put down his cheap yellow suitcase.
===Chinese===
塞莉娜是知情的。那天晚上八点，珀维斯进城赶集未归，鲁尔夫来敲农舍的门，一拧门把进了屋，同往常一样。可他这副模样，却没有一处像往常。他穿着他最好的一身衣裳——他头一套铺子里买的现成衣裳，是办母亲丧事那阵子置办的。那衣裳打一开始就不合身，如今更是小得不成样子。这八九个月里，他蹿个儿蹿得吓人。然而此刻站在她面前，他高挑、精瘦、黝黑，没有一丝可笑的地方。他放下那只便宜的黄色小提箱。

===Original===
“Well, Roelf.”
===Chinese===
「哎，鲁尔夫。」

===Original===
“I am going away. I couldn’t stay.”
===Chinese===
「我要走了。我待不下去了。」

===Original===
She nodded. “Where?”
===Chinese===
她点点头。「去哪儿？」

===Original===
“Away. Chicago maybe.” He was terribly moved, so he made his tone casual. “They came home last night. I have got some books that belong to you.” He made as though to open the suitcase.
===Chinese===
「远走。兴许去芝加哥。」他心里翻江倒海，偏把口气放得随随便便。「他们昨儿夜里回来了。我有几本书是你的。」他作势要开那箱子。

===Original===
“No, no! Keep them.”
===Chinese===
「不，不！留着吧。」

===Original===
“Goodbye.”
===Chinese===
「再见。」

===Original===
“Goodbye, Roelf.” She took the boy’s dark head in her two hands and, standing on tiptoe, kissed him. He turned to go. “Wait a minute. Wait a minute.” She had a few dollars﻿—in quarters, dimes, half dollars﻿—perhaps ten dollars in all﻿—hidden away in a canister on the shelf. She reached for it. But when she came back with the box in her hand he was gone.
===Chinese===
「再见，鲁尔夫。」她双手捧住男孩黝黑的头，踮起脚尖，吻了他。他转身要走。「等等。等一等。」她还有几个钱——两毛五的、一毛的、五毛的——统共兴许有十来块——收在搁板上一只小罐里。她伸手去取。可等她捧着罐子回来，人已经走了。

## IX / 第九章

===Original===
Dirk was eight; Little Sobig DeJong, in a suit made of bean-sacking sewed together by his mother. A brown blond boy with mosquito bites on his legs and his legs never still. Nothing of the dreamer about this lad. The one-room schoolhouse of Selina’s day had been replaced by a two-story brick structure, very fine, of which High Prairie was vastly proud. The rusty iron stove had been dethroned by a central heater. Dirk went to school from October until June. Pervus protested that this was foolish. The boy could be of great help in the fields from the beginning of April to the first of November, but Selina fought savagely for his schooling, and won.
===Chinese===
德克八岁了；小「这么大」德容穿着一身衣裳——豆袋布裁的，他母亲一针一线缝的。一个晒得褐黄的金发男孩，两条腿上尽是蚊子咬的包，也尽是安分不下来的劲儿。这孩子身上没有半点梦想家的气质。塞莉娜当年那间独屋校舍，如今已换成一幢两层的砖楼，非常气派，高草原人为它得意得了不得。生了锈的铁炉子已被中央取暖炉取而代之。德克从十月上学一直上到来年六月。珀维斯抗议说这是犯傻。从四月初到十一月初，这孩子在田里本可以帮上大忙，可塞莉娜为了他上学凶悍地拼争，并且赢了。

===Original===
“Reading and writing and figgering is what a farmer is got to know,” Pervus argued. “The rest is all foolishness. Constantinople is the capital of Turkey he studies last night and uses good oil in the lamp. What good does it do a truck farmer when he knows Constantinople is the capital of Turkey? That don’t help him raise turnips.”
===Chinese===
「读书，写字，算账——庄稼人懂这几样就尽够了，」珀维斯争辩道。「其余的全是犯傻。他昨晚烧着好灯油，念什么君士坦丁堡是土耳其的京城。一个种菜的知道君士坦丁堡是土耳其的京城，于他有什么好处？那可不能帮他多种萝卜。」

===Original===
“Sobig isn’t a truck farmer.”
===Chinese===
「『这么大』可不是种菜的。」

===Original===
“Well, he will be pretty soon. Time I was fifteen I was running our place.”
===Chinese===
「哼，他很快就得当了。我十五岁的时候就当着家打理咱们的地了。」

===Original===
Verbally Selina did not combat this. But within her every force was gathering to fight it when the time should come. Her Sobig a truck farmer, a slave to the soil, bent by it, beaten by it, blasted by it, so that he, in time, like the other men of High Prairie, would take on the very look of the rocks and earth among which they toiled!
===Chinese===
嘴上，塞莉娜没有同他争。可是在她身体里，每一股力量都在集结，只等时候一到便向这话开战。她的「这么大」当个种菜的，做泥土的奴隶，被它压弯，被它捶打，被它摧残，到得将来，变得和高草原其余的男人一模一样，染上他们终日劳碌其间的岩石与泥土的那副形容！

===Original===
Dirk, at eight, was a none too handsome child, considering his father and mother﻿—or his father and mother as they had been. He had, though, a “different” look. His eyelashes were too long for a boy. Wasted, Selina said as she touched them with a fond forefinger, when a girl would have been so glad of them. He had developed, too, a slightly aquiline nose, probably a long-jump inheritance from some Cromwellian rapscallion of the English Peakes of a past century. It was not until he was seventeen or eighteen that he was to metamorphose suddenly into a graceful and aristocratic youngster with an indefinable look about him of distinction and actual elegance. It was when Dirk was thirty that Peter Peel the English tailor (of Michigan Avenue north) said he was the only man in Chicago who could wear English clothes without having them look like Halsted Street. Dirk probably appeared a little startled at that, as well he might, west Halsted Street having loomed up so large in his background.
===Chinese===
德克八岁这年，论相貌实在算不上俊——想想他的父母，或者说想想他父母当年的模样。不过他有一股「与众不同」的神气。他的睫毛，对一个男孩来说太长了。可惜了，塞莉娜用宠爱的食指抚着它们说，要是个女孩，还不知怎么宝贝呢。他还长出了微微的鹰钩鼻，多半是一记隔了好几代的远跳，从上世纪英国皮克家某一支的克伦威尔式泼皮那里传下来的。要到十七八岁上，他才会蓦然蜕变成一个俊雅而贵胄气派的少年，周身透着一股说不出的出众与真正的优雅。德克三十岁那年，英国裁缝彼得·皮尔（铺子在北密歇根大道，Michigan Avenue）说，全芝加哥只有他一个男人，穿英国衣裳能不穿出一股哈尔斯特德街的味道。德克听了这话多半显得有点吃惊，他原也有理由吃惊，要知道西哈尔斯特德街在他的背景里可是赫赫一方。

===Original===
Selina was a farm woman now, nearing thirty. The work rode her as it had ridden Maartje Pool. In the DeJong yard there was always a dado of washing, identical with the one that had greeted Selina’s eye when first she drove into the Pool yard years before. Faded overalls, a shirt, socks, a boy’s drawers grotesquely patched and mended, towels of rough sacking. She, too, rose at four, snatched up shapeless garments, invested herself with them, seized her great coil of fine cloudy hair, twisted it into a utilitarian knob and skewered it with a hairpin from which the varnish had long departed, leaving it a dull gray; thrust her slim feet into shapeless shoes, dabbed her face with cold water, hurried to the kitchen stove. The work was always at her heels, its breath hot on her neck. Baskets of mending piled up, threatened to overwhelm her. Overalls, woollen shirts, drawers, socks. Socks! They lay coiled and twisted in an old market basket. Sometimes as she sat late at night mending them, in and out, in and out, with quick fierce stabs of the needle in her work-scarred hand, they seemed to writhe and squirm and wriggle horribly, like snakes. One of her bad dreams was that in which she saw herself overwhelmed, drowned, swallowed up by a huge welter and boiling of undarned, unmended nightshirts, drawers, socks, aprons, overalls.
===Chinese===
塞莉娜如今是个农妇了，年近三十。活计骑在她身上，一如当年骑在玛尔切·普尔身上。德容家的院子里总挂着一圈墙裙似的换洗衣裳，同多年前她头一回赶车进普尔家院子时迎面撞见的那一挂一模一样。褪色的工装裤，一件衬衫，几双袜子，一条补得七扭八歪的男孩衬裤，粗麻袋布的毛巾。她也四点钟起身，抓起没模样的衣裳，胡乱裹在身上，拢住她那一大卷细云似的头发，拧成一个只图实用的髻，用一根发钗别住——钗子上的漆早已剥落净尽，只剩一团暗灰；把纤瘦的脚塞进没模样的鞋里，往脸上撩几把冷水，就奔厨房的炉子去。活计永远跟在她脚跟后头，喷着热气咬她的后颈。一筐筐的缝补越堆越高，险些把她埋了。工装裤、羊毛衬衫、衬裤、袜子。袜子！它们盘绕扭结在一只旧菜筐里。有时她深夜坐在那儿补袜子，一针进，一针出，一针进，一针出，结着劳痕的手里针脚又急又狠，那些袜子便仿佛怕人地蠕动、扭绞、蜿蜒起来，像蛇。她的噩梦之一，便是看见自己被一大锅沸反盈天、无人织补的睡衣、衬裤、袜子、围裙、工装裤压倒、淹没、吞噬。

===Original===
Seeing her thus one would have thought that the Selina Peake of the wine-red cashmere, the fun-loving disposition, the high-spirited courage, had departed forever. But these things still persisted. For that matter, even the wine-red cashmere clung to existence. So hopelessly old-fashioned now as to be almost picturesque, it hung in Selina’s closet like a rosy memory. Sometimes when she came upon it in an orgy of cleaning she would pass her rough hands over its soft folds and by that magic process Mrs. Pervus DeJong vanished in a pouf and in her place was the girl Selina Peake perched a-tiptoe on a soapbox in Adam Ooms’s hall while all High Prairie, open-mouthed, looked on as the impecunious Pervus DeJong threw ten hard-earned dollars at her feet. In thrifty moments she had often thought of cutting the wine-red cashmere into rag-rug strips; of dyeing it a sedate brown or black and remodelling it for a much-needed best dress; of fashioning it into shirts for Dirk. But she never did.
===Chinese===
见她这副情形，谁都会以为，那个穿酒红色开司米的塞莉娜·皮克——那个爱热闹的性子、意气飞扬的胆气——已经一去不返了。可这些东西其实都还在。不要说别的，就连那件酒红色开司米也还倔强地存活着。如今老派得没救，倒几乎称得上一幅画了，它挂在塞莉娜的衣柜里，像一段玫瑰色的回忆。有时大扫除扫得性起撞见它，她会把粗糙的双手抚上那柔软的衣褶，经此一番魔法，珀维斯·德容太太噗的一声化为乌有，站在原处的是姑娘塞莉娜·皮克，踮着脚尖立在亚当·奥姆斯家厅堂里的一只肥皂箱上，全高草原的人张着嘴围观，看那不名一文的珀维斯·德容把十块血汗钱丢在她脚边。节俭心发作的时候，她不止一次想过把这件酒红色开司米裁成碎布地毯的布条；把它染成持重的棕色或黑色，改一件急需的体面衣裳；或是给德克做几件衬衫。可她从来没有。

===Original===
It would be gratifying to be able to record that in these eight or nine years Selina had been able to work wonders on the DeJong farm; that the house glittered, the crops thrived richly, the barn housed sleek cattle. But it could not be truthfully said. True, she had achieved some changes, but at the cost of terrific effort. A less indomitable woman would have sunk into apathy years before. The house had a coat of paint﻿—lead-gray, because it was cheapest. There were two horses﻿—the second a broken-down old mare, blind in one eye, that they had picked up for five dollars after it had been turned out to pasture for future sale as horse-carcass. Piet Pon, the mare’s owner who drove a milk route, had hoped to get three dollars for the animal, dead. A month of rest and pasturage restored the mare to usefulness. Selina had made the bargain, and Pervus had scolded her roundly for it. Now he drove the mare to market, saw that she pulled more sturdily than the other horse, but had never retracted. It was no quality of meanness in him. Pervus merely was like that.
===Chinese===
这八九年间，若能记下塞莉娜在德容家农场创出了怎样的奇迹——家宅熠熠生辉，庄稼茂盛丰收，牲口棚里圈着毛色油亮的牲口——那自然叫人欣慰。可照实说，不能这么讲。不错，她确实带来了一些改变，却是拿惊人的苦功换来的。换个不那么百折不挠的女人，多年前就该沉入麻木了。房子刷过一遍漆——铅灰色，因为顶便宜。家里有两匹马——第二匹是匹垮了架的老母马，一只眼瞎，是它被赶进牧场等着日后当马肉卖的时候，他们花五块钱捡来的。母马的主人皮特·庞（Piet Pon）赶一条送奶的路线，原指望这牲口死了还能换三块钱。歇了一个月、放了一个月的草，这母马重新顶了用。这笔买卖是塞莉娜讲下的价，为这珀维斯把她狠狠数落了一顿。如今他赶着这母马上市场，眼见它拉得比另一匹还稳实，却从没收回过那番话。这倒不是他刻薄。珀维斯就是这么个人。

===Original===
But the west sixteen! That had been Selina’s most heroic achievement. Her plan, spoken of to Pervus in the first month of her marriage, had taken years to mature; even now was but a partial triumph. She had even descended to nagging.
===Chinese===
可是西边那十六英亩！那才是塞莉娜最英雄的功业。她的这个计划，婚后头一个月就对珀维斯说过的，费了若干年才算成熟；直到如今也不过成了一半。她甚至不惜堕落到唠叨。

===Original===
“Why don’t we put in asparagus?”
===Chinese===
「咱们干吗不种芦笋？」

===Original===
“Asparagus!” considered something of a luxury, and rarely included in the High Prairie truck farmer’s products. “And wait three years for a crop!”
===Chinese===
「芦笋！」——这东西给当成半件奢侈品，高草原菜农的产品里难得有它。「还得等三年才见收成！」

===Original===
“Yes, but then we’d have it. And a plantation’s good for ten years, once it’s started.”
===Chinese===
「是，可打那以后咱们就年年有了。而且芦笋园一旦立起来，好好能收个十年。」

===Original===
“Plantation! What is that? An asparagus plantation? Asparagus I’ve always heard of in beds.”
===Chinese===
「园！什么话？芦笋园？我一辈子听说芦笋都是种在畦里的。」

===Original===
“That’s the old idea. I’ve been reading up on it. The new way is to plant asparagus in rows, the way you would rhubarb or corn. Plant six feet apart, and four acres anyway.”
===Chinese===
「那是老观念了。我查过不少书。新法子是把芦笋种成行，就像种大黄、种玉米那样。行距六英尺，起码种四英亩。」

===Original===
He was not even sufficiently interested to be amused. “Yeh, four acres where? In the clay land, maybe.” He did laugh then, if the short bitter sound he made could be construed as indicating mirth. “Out of a book.”
===Chinese===
他连觉得好笑的兴趣都提不起来。「呃，四英亩，种哪儿？种黏土地里呗，大概。」他倒是笑了——如果从他嗓子里挤出来的那声短促的苦响也能算笑的话。「书上来的玩意儿。」

===Original===
“In the clay land,” Selina urged, crisply. “And out of a book. Every farmer in High Prairie raises cabbage, turnips, carrots, beets, beans, onions, and they’re better quality than ours. That west sixteen isn’t bringing you anything, so what difference does it make if I am wrong! Let me put my own money into it, I’ve thought it all out, Pervus. Please. We’ll under-drain the clay soil. Just five or six acres, to start. We’ll manure it heavily﻿—as much as we can afford﻿—and then for two years we’ll plant potatoes there. We’ll put in our asparagus plants the third spring﻿—one-year-old seedlings. I’ll promise to keep it weeded﻿—Dirk and I. He’ll be a big boy by that time.”
===Chinese===
「就种黏土地，」塞莉娜干脆利落地劝。「就照书上来的。高草原家家的菜农都种卷心菜、萝卜、胡萝卜、甜菜、豆子、洋葱，样样比咱家的成色好。那西边十六亩反正一个子儿也不给你挣，就算我错了又碍着什么！让我拿自己的钱往里投，我全都盘算好了，珀维斯。求你了。咱们给那黏土地下瓦管排水。先来五英亩，至多六英亩。咱们往里猛上粪——能上多少上多少——然后连着两年在那儿种土豆。第三年开春把芦笋苗栽下去——一年的秧苗。我担保把草除净——我和德克两个。到那时候他就是个大孩子了。」

===Original===
“How much manure?”
===Chinese===
「上多少粪？」

===Original===
“Oh, twenty to forty tons to the acre﻿—”
===Chinese===
「哦，一英亩二十到四十吨——」

===Original===
He shook his head in slow Dutch opposition.
===Chinese===
他摇头，缓慢地、荷兰人式地不赞成。

===Original===
“﻿—but if you’ll let me use humus I won’t need that much. Let me try it, Pervus. Let me try.”
===Chinese===
「——可你若准我用腐殖土，就用不着那么多。让我试一试，珀维斯。让我试试。」

===Original===
In the end she had her way, partly because Pervus was too occupied with his own endless work to oppose her; and partly because he was, in his undemonstrative way, still in love with his vivacious, nimble-witted, high-spirited wife, though to her frantic goadings and proddings he was as phlegmatically oblivious as an elephant to a pin prick. Year in, year out, he maintained his slow-plodding gait, content to do as his father had done before him; content to let the rest of High Prairie pass him on the road. He rarely showed temper. Selina often wished he would. Sometimes, in a sort of hysteria of hopelessness, she would rush at him, ruffle up his thick coarse hair, now beginning to be threaded with gray; shake his great impassive shoulders.
===Chinese===
到头来她还是遂了愿，一半因为珀维斯自家没完没了的活计缠身，腾不出手来拦她；一半因为以他不露声色的方式，他仍旧爱着他这个活泼鲜灵、脑子伶俐、意气飞扬的妻子——虽说她连催带戳、急得发疯，他却浑然不觉，淡定得像大象挨了根针扎。年复一年，他保持着他那迟缓沉拖的步子，满足于照他父亲的老法子行事；满足于让高草原其余的人在路上一个个超过去。他难得动怒。塞莉娜倒常常巴望他动怒。有时，在一种绝望之极的癔症里，她会朝他扑过去，揉乱他一头浓密的粗发——那头发已开始夹起白丝了；摇撼他副宽阔而无动于衷的肩膀。

===Original===
“Pervus! Pervus! if you’d only get mad﻿—real mad! Fly into a rage. Break things! Beat me! Sell the farm! Run away!” She didn’t mean it, of course. It was the vital and constructive force in her resenting his apathy, his acceptance of things as they were.
===Chinese===
「珀维斯！珀维斯！你哪怕动一回气——动一回真格的气！发一顿雷霆。砸东西！打我！把农场卖了！跑！」当然，她并不是当真的。那是她体内那股生命的、要营建的力量，在怨恨他的麻木不仁，怨恨他对万事照单全收。

===Original===
“What is that for dumb talk?” He would regard her solemnly through a haze of smoke, his pipe making a maddening putt-putt of sleepy content.
===Chinese===
「说这种呆话做什么？」他会隔着一重烟雾，郑重其事地端详她，烟斗咕嘟咕嘟地响着，一副餍足的困倦，响得叫人发疯。

===Original===
Though she worked as hard as any woman in High Prairie, had as little, dressed as badly, he still regarded her as a luxury; an exquisite toy which, in a moment of madness, he had taken for himself. “Little Lina”﻿—tolerantly, fondly. You would have thought that he spoiled her, pampered her. Perhaps he even thought he did.
===Chinese===
她干得跟高草原随便哪个女人一样苦，一样一无所有，穿得一样寒碜，他却仍旧把她看作一件奢侈品；一件他在一时癫狂里给自己弄到手的精巧玩物。「小莉娜」——纵容地、疼爱地。你会以为他宠她、娇她。说不定他自己也这么以为。

===Original===
When she spoke of modern farming, of books on vegetable gardening, he came very near to angry impatience, though his amusement at the idea saved him from it. College agricultural courses he designated as foolishness. Of Linnaeus he had never heard. Burbank was, for him, nonexistent, and he thought head-lettuce a silly fad. Selina sometimes talked of raising this last named green as a salad, with marketing value. Everyone knew that regular lettuce was leaf lettuce which you ate with vinegar and a sprinkling of sugar, or with hot bacon and fat sopping its wilted leaves.
===Chinese===
每逢她讲起现代农业、讲起种菜的园艺书，他就几乎要气急败坏——多亏这念头本身逗乐了他，才没发作。大学里的农学课程，他一概断为犯傻。林奈（Linnaeus）这个名字，他从没听人说起过。伯班克（Burbank）于他根本不存在，结球生菜（head-lettuce）在他看来更是无聊的一时风尚。塞莉娜有时讲起要种这后一样绿菜当沙拉，说它有市场价值。人人都知道，正经的生菜是散叶的，得就着醋和一撮糖吃，或是让滚烫的培根连着油浇上去，泡透那蔫软的叶子。

===Original===
He said, too, she spoiled the boy. Back of this may have been a lurking jealousy. “Always the boy; always the boy,” he would mutter when Selina planned for the child; shielded him; took his part (sometimes unjustly). “You will make a softy of him with your always babying.” So from time to time he undertook to harden Dirk. The result was generally disastrous. In one case the process terminated in what was perilously near to tragedy. It was during the midsummer school vacation. Dirk was eight. The woody slopes about High Prairie and the sand hills beyond were covered with the rich blue of huckleberries. They were dead ripe. One shower would spoil them. Geertje and Jozina Pool were going huckleberrying and had consented to take Dirk﻿—a concession, for he was only eight and considered, at their advanced age, a tagger. But the last of the tomatoes on the DeJong place were also ripe and ready for picking. They hung, firm, juicy scarlet globes, prime for the Chicago market. Pervus meant to haul them to town that day. And this was work in which the boy could help. To Dirk’s, “Can I go berrying? The huckleberries are ripe. Geert and Jozina are going,” his father shook a negative head.
===Chinese===
他还说，她惯坏了那孩子。这话背后兴许蛰伏着一丝妒意。「总是孩子，总是孩子，」每当塞莉娜替这孩子打算、护着他、偏帮他（有时偏帮得不讲道理），他就要这么咕哝。「你这么一天到晚惯着他，要把他惯成个软蛋。」于是他时不时地着手去磨砺德克。结果往往是一场灾难。有一回，这番磨砺收场收得险些成了悲剧。那是仲夏的学校假期。德克八岁。高草原四下的林间坡地和更远处的沙丘，铺满了越橘（huckleberry）浓重的蓝色。熟得透透的了。一场阵雨就能毁了它们。海尔切和约西娜·普尔要去采越橘，总算肯带上德克——这已是莫大的迁就，因为他才八岁，在她们那把高龄看来，纯是个跟屁虫。可德容家地里的最后一批番茄也熟了，正等着摘。一颗颗结实、多汁的红球挂在那儿，正是发往芝加哥市场的一等成色。珀维斯打算那天把它们拉进城。而这活儿，那孩子也帮得上手。德克说：「我能去采浆果吗？越橘熟了。海尔切和约西娜都去。」他父亲摇头，不许。

===Original===
“Yes, well tomatoes are ripe, too, and that comes before huckleberries. There’s the whole patch to clean up this afternoon by four.”
===Chinese===
「行了，番茄也熟了，番茄排在越橘前头。今天下午四点以前，有一整块地要摘利索。」

===Original===
Selina looked up, glanced at Pervus’s face, at the boy’s, said nothing. The look said, “He’s a child. Let him go, Pervus.”
===Chinese===
塞莉娜抬眼望了望，瞥一眼珀维斯的脸，再瞥一眼孩子的脸，什么也没说。那眼神在说：「他还是个孩子。放他去吧，珀维斯。」

===Original===
Dirk flushed with disappointment. They were at breakfast. It was barely daybreak. He looked down at his plate, his lip quivered, his long lashes lay heavy on his cheeks. Pervus got up, wiped his mouth with the back of his hand. There was a hard day ahead of him. “Time I was your age, Sobig, I would think it was an easy day when all I had to do was pick a tomato patch clean.”
===Chinese===
德克失望得涨红了脸。他们正吃着早饭。天才蒙蒙亮。他低头看着盘子，嘴唇发颤，长长的睫毛沉沉压在腮上。珀维斯站起来，用手背抹了抹嘴。等着他的是劳苦的一天。「像你这么大的时候，『这么大』，哪天要是只消把一块番茄地摘干净，我就觉得那算是轻省日子了。」

===Original===
Dirk looked up then, quickly. “If I get it all picked can I go?”
===Chinese===
德克这才猛地抬头。「要是我全摘完了，我能去吗？」

===Original===
“It’s a day’s job.”
===Chinese===
「那是一整天的活儿。」

===Original===
“But if I do pick the patch﻿—if I get through early enough﻿—can I go?”
===Chinese===
「可要是我真把地摘完了——要是我干得够早完事——我能去吗？」

===Original===
In his mind’s eye Pervus saw the tomato patch, more scarlet than green, so thick hung the fruit upon the bushes. He smiled. “Yes. You pick them tomatoes and you can go. But no throwing into the baskets and getting ’em all softed up.”
===Chinese===
珀维斯心里的眼睛看见那块番茄地，红多过绿，果实把株棵压得密密匝匝。他笑了。「行。你把那些番茄摘完，就可以去。不过不许往筐里扔，砸得稀烂软塌。」

===Original===
Secretly Selina resolved to help him, but she knew that this could not be until afternoon. The berry patches were fully three miles from the DeJong farm. Dirk would have to finish by three o’clock, at the latest, to get there. Selina had her morning full with the housework.
===Chinese===
塞莉娜暗暗打定主意帮他，可她知道不到下午脱不开身。浆果地离德容家农场足足三英里。德克最迟三点钟就得干完，才赶得到那儿。塞莉娜一上午的家务排得满满的。

===Original===
He was in the patch before six; fell to work, feverishly. He picked, heaped the fruit into hillocks. The scarlet patches glowed, bloodred, in the sun. The child worked like a machine, with an economy of gesture calculated to the fraction of an inch. He picked, stooped, heaped the mounds in the sultry heat of the August morning. The sweat stood out on his forehead, darkened his blond hair, slid down his cheeks that were pink, then red, then tinged with a purplish tone beneath the summer tan. When dinner time came he gulped a dozen alarming mouthfuls and was out again in the broiling noonday glare. Selina left her dinner dishes unwashed on the table to help him, but Pervus intervened. “The boy’s got to do it alone,” he insisted.
===Chinese===
不到六点他就下了地；一头扎进活计里，干得发狠。他摘着，把果实堆成小丘。一堆堆猩红在太阳底下烧得血红。这孩子干活像一架机器，每个动作都精打细算到几分之一英寸。他在八月清晨的闷热里摘果、弯腰、堆垄。汗珠从他额头上冒出来，浸黑了他的金发，顺着脸颊往下淌——那脸颊先是粉红，继而通红，最后在夏日的晒色底下透出一层紫。晌午饭点到了，他狼吞虎咽塞下十几口吓人的饭，又冲回正午白晃晃的酷日里。塞莉娜撂下午饭没洗的碗碟要去帮他，珀维斯却拦住了。「这活儿必须他一个人干完，」他坚持道。

===Original===
“He’ll never do it, Pervus. He’s only eight.”
===Chinese===
「他干不完的，珀维斯。他才八岁。」

===Original===
“Time I was eight﻿—”
===Chinese===
「我八岁的时候——」

===Original===
He actually had cleared the patch by three. He went to the well and took a huge draught of water; drank two great dippersful, lipping it down thirstily, like a colt. It was cool and delicious beyond belief. Then he sloshed a third and a fourth dipperful over his hot head and neck, took an empty lard pail for berries and was off down the dusty road and across the fields, running fleetly in spite of the quivering heat waves that seemed to dance between fiery heaven and parched earth. Selina stood in the kitchen doorway a moment, watching him. He looked very small and determined.
===Chinese===
到三点钟，他果真把那块地摘完了。他走到井边，灌了一大口水；喝下满满两瓢，像匹小马驹似地就着瓢沿贪渴地咂饮。那水凉得出奇、甘美得难以置信。接着他又舀了第三瓢、第四瓢，哗哗浇在滚烫的头上、脖子上，拎起一只装猪油的空桶当浆果桶，就顺着尘土飞扬的大路、穿过田野跑远了——任凭灼热的天与焦渴的地之间热浪颤动如舞，他依旧跑得飞快。塞莉娜在厨房门口站了一会儿，望着他。他的背影显得那么小，又那么决绝。

===Original===
He found Geertje and Jozina, surfeited with fruit, berry stained and bramble torn, lolling languidly in Kuyper’s woods. He began to pick the plump blue balls but he ate them listlessly, though thriftily, because that was what he had come for and his father was Dutch. When Geertje and Jozina prepared to leave not an hour after he had come he was ready to go, yet curiously loath to move. His lard pail was half filled. He trotted home laboriously through the late afternoon, feeling giddy and sick, with horrid pains in his head. That night he tossed in delirium, begged not to be made lie down, came perilously near to death.
===Chinese===
他找到海尔切和约西娜时，两人已吃浆果吃腻了，染着一身果渍、带着一身荆棘划痕，懒洋洋地歪在凯珀家的树林里。他动手摘那些圆鼓鼓的蓝果珠，却吃得没精打采，尽管吃得很俭省——因为他是为采它们而来的，也因为他的父亲是荷兰人。他到后还不到一个钟头，海尔切和约西娜就准备回家了，他也正好走得了，心里却奇怪地懒得动弹。他的猪油桶装了半桶。傍晚时分，他吃力地一路小跑着回了家，只觉得头晕眼花、浑身难受，头一阵阵疼得吓人。当天夜里他便烧得说起胡话，翻腾挣扎，哀求别让他躺下，一度险些丢了性命。

===Original===
Selina’s heart was an engine pumping terror, hate, agony through her veins. Hate for her husband who had done this to the boy.
===Chinese===
塞莉娜的心成了一台引擎，把恐怖、仇恨、剧痛泵进她的血管。恨她的丈夫——是他把孩子害成这样。

===Original===
“You did it! You did it! He’s a baby and you made him work like a man. If anything happens to him! If anything happens to him!﻿—”
===Chinese===
「是你干的！是你干的！他还是个小娃娃，你却逼他像大人一样干活。他要是有个三长两短！他要是有个三长两短！——」

===Original===
“Well, I didn’t think the kid would go for to do it. I didn’t ask him to pick and then go berrying. He said could he and I said yes. If I had said no it would have been wrong, too, maybe.”
===Chinese===
「这……我没想到这小子真会去干完。我没叫他摘完地再去采浆果。是他问我行不行，我说行。要是我说不行，兴许也一样是错的。」

===Original===
“You’re all alike. Look at Roelf Pool! They tried to make a farmer of him, too. And ruined him.”
===Chinese===
「你们全都一个样。看看鲁尔夫·普尔！他们也想过把他造就成个庄稼人。结果毁了他。」

===Original===
“What’s the matter with farming? What’s the matter with a farmer? You said farm work was grand work, once.”
===Chinese===
「种地怎么了？庄稼人怎么了？你从前不是说过，田里的活儿是顶了不起的活儿吗。」

===Original===
“Oh, I did. It is. It could be. It﻿—Oh, what’s the use of talking like that now! Look at him! Don’t, Sobig! Don’t, baby. How hot his head is! Listen! Is that Jan with the doctor? No. No, it isn’t. Mustard plasters. Are you sure that’s the right thing?”
===Chinese===
「哦，我是说过。它是。它本可以是的。它——哦，这会儿说这些还有什么用！看看他！别这样，「这么大」！别这样，宝贝。他脑袋多烫啊！听！是扬带着大夫回来了吗？不。不，不是。芥末膏。你敢肯定那东西对症吗？」

===Original===
It was before the day of the omnipresent farmhouse telephone and the farmhouse Ford. Jan’s trip to High Prairie village for the doctor and back to the farm meant a delay of hours. But within two days the boy was again about, rather pale, but otherwise seeming none the worse for his experience.
===Chinese===
那还是农家电话与农家福特车无处不在的年代之前。扬跑一趟高草原村镇去请大夫、再回农场，一去就是好几个钟头。可不出两天，那孩子又起来了，脸色发白，别的倒像是没什么大碍。

===Original===
That was Pervus. Thrifty, like his kind, but unlike them in shrewdness. Penny wise, pound foolish; a characteristic that brought him his death. September, usually a succession of golden days and hazy opalescent evenings on the Illinois prairie land, was disastrously cold and rainy that year. Pervus’s great frame was racked by rheumatism. He was forty now, and over, still of magnificent physique, so that to see him suffering gave Selina the pangs of pity that one has at sight of the very strong or the very weak in pain. He drove the weary miles to market three times a week, for September was the last big month of the truck farmer’s season. After that only the hardier plants survived the frosts﻿—the cabbages, beets, turnips, carrots, pumpkins, squash. The roads in places were morasses of mud into which the wheels were likely to sink to the hubs. Once stuck you had often to wait for a friendly passing team to haul you out. Pervus would start early, detour for miles in order to avoid the worst places. Jan was too stupid, too old, too inexpert to be trusted with the Haymarket trading. Selina would watch Pervus drive off down the road in the creaking old market wagon, the green stuff protected by canvas, but Pervus wet before ever he climbed into the seat. There never seemed to be enough waterproof canvas for both.
===Chinese===
珀维斯就是这样的人。跟他同族的人一样节俭，却没有他们的精明。小事精明，大事糊涂；正是这个脾性送了他的命。九月，在伊利诺伊的草原上本是连绵的金色白昼和雾蒙蒙的乳彩黄昏，那一年却冷得出奇、雨得出奇。珀维斯高大的身躯被风湿痛折磨着。他如今四十出头了，体格依然雄伟，所以看他受苦，塞莉娜心里便涌起那种看到极强壮或极孱弱者遭罪时才有的、锥心一样的怜悯。他每礼拜三趟赶那累人的几十里路进城卖菜，因为九月是菜农这一季最后的大月。此后经得起霜冻的只剩那些更耐寒的作物——卷心菜、甜菜、萝卜、胡萝卜、南瓜、西葫芦。路上有些地段是烂泥塘，车轮动不动就陷到轮毂。一旦陷住，往往只好等一辆好心的过路马车来拖你出去。珀维斯总是早早动身，宁可绕出好几里地，避开最糟的路段。扬呢，太蠢、太老、太不在行，海马克特上的交易放心交不得给他。塞莉娜常看着珀维斯赶着那辆吱呀作响的旧菜车顺大路去了，青菜绿菜用帆布苫着，可珀维斯还没爬上座位，人已经先淋湿了。防水帆布从来就不够两个人用似的。

===Original===
“Pervus, take it off those sacks and put it over your shoulders.”
===Chinese===
「珀维斯，把帆布从那些麻袋上拿下来，披到你肩膀上。」

===Original===
“That’s them white globe onions. The last of ’em. I can get a fancy price for them but not if they’re all wetted down.”
===Chinese===
「那是白圆葱。最后一批了。我能卖个好价钱，可要是全淋湿了就不行了。」

===Original===
“Don’t sleep on the wagon tonight, Pervus. Sleep in. Be sure. It saves in the end. You know the last time you were laid up for a week.”
===Chinese===
「今晚别睡在车上了，珀维斯。找个屋里睡。千万。到头来是省钱的。上回你一躺就是一个礼拜，你知道的。」

===Original===
“It’ll clear. Breaking now over there in the west.”
===Chinese===
「天要放晴了。这会儿西边正在裂开呢。」

===Original===
The clouds did break late in the afternoon; the false sun came out hot and bright. Pervus slept out in the Haymarket, for the night was close and humid. At midnight the lake wind sprang up, cold and treacherous, and with it came the rain again. Pervus was drenched by morning, chilled, thoroughly miserable. A hot cup of coffee at four and another at ten when the rush of trading was over stimulated him but little. When he reached home it was mid-afternoon. Beneath the bronze wrought by the wind and sun of many years the gray-white of sickness shone dully, like silver under enamel. Selina put him to bed against his halfhearted protests. Banked him with hot water jars, a hot iron wrapped in flannel at his feet. But later came fever instead of the expected relief of perspiration. Ill though he was he looked more ruddy and hale than most men in health; but suddenly Selina, startled, saw black lines like gashes etched under his eyes, about his mouth, in his cheeks.
===Chinese===
云到傍晚后段果然裂开了；那骗人的太阳钻了出来，又毒又亮。珀维斯在海马克特露宿，因为夜里闷热潮湿。午夜，湖上来的风骤起，阴冷而奸诈，跟着雨又来了。到早晨珀维斯已浑身湿透，寒透，狼狈至极。四点钟一杯热咖啡，十点钟买卖高峰过去又一杯，也没能给他多少提携。到家时已是下半晌。多年风霜日头铸下的古铜色底下，病态的灰白幽幽地泛出来，像珐琅下面的银。塞莉娜不顾他有气无力的推拒，把他安顿上床。给他掖上热水罐，脚边放一块法兰绒裹着的烧火铁。可随后等来的不是指望中的发汗轻快，而是发烧。他病归病，看上去却比多数健康人还红润硬朗；可是忽然间，塞莉娜吃了一惊，看见他眼下、嘴边、面颊上，蚀刻出一道道黑线，像刀口。

===Original===
In a day when pneumonia was known as lung fever and in a locality that advised closed windows and hot air as a remedy, Pervus’s battle was lost before the doctor’s hooded buggy was seen standing in the yard for long hours through the night. Toward morning the doctor had Jan Steen stable the horse. It was a sultry night, with flashes of heat lightning in the west.
===Chinese===
在那个人们还把肺炎唤作「肺热病」（lung fever）、当地把紧闭门窗和热气奉为灵方的时候，珀维斯这一仗早败了——夜里有好几个钟头，大夫那辆带篷的轻便马车便一直停在场院里。快天亮时，大夫让扬·斯汀把马牵进牲口棚。那是个闷热的夜，西天不时闪着无雨的热闪。

===Original===
“I should think if you opened the windows,” Selina said to the old High Prairie doctor over and over, emboldened by terror, “it would help him to breathe. He﻿—he’s breathing so﻿—he’s breathing so﻿—” She could not bring herself to say so terribly. The sound of the words wrung her as did the sound of his terrible breathing.
===Chinese===
「我想您要是把窗子打开，」塞莉娜被恐惧壮着胆，一遍又一遍地对那位高草原的老大夫说，「总该能帮他喘口气。他——他喘成那样——他喘成那样——」那个「可怕」，她终究没能说出口。那几个字的声音绞着她的心，正如他那可怕的喘息声绞着她的心。

## X / 第十章

===Original===
Perhaps the most poignant and touching feature of the days that followed was not the sight of this stricken giant, lying majestic and aloof in his unwonted black; nor of the boy Dirk, mystified but elated, too, with the unaccustomed stir and excitement; nor of the shabby little farm that seemed to shrink and dwindle into further insignificance beneath the sudden publicity turned upon it. No; it was the sight of Selina, widowed, but having no time for decent tears. The farm was there; it must be tended. Illness, death, sorrow﻿—the garden must be tended, the vegetables pulled, hauled to market, sold. Upon the garden depended the boy’s future, and hers.
===Chinese===
此后的那些日子里，最令人心酸、最动人的一幕，倒不是那巨人般的死者身遭大变，穿着他不惯穿的一身黑衣，庄严而孤高地躺在那里；也不是男孩德克，既惘然又兴奋，被这不寻常的骚动与忙乱弄得晕头转向；也不是这座寒酸的小农庄，在骤然聚焦的众目睽睽之下，仿佛缩了缩身，愈发显得卑微琐屑。都不是；是塞莉娜的身影——她做了寡妇，却没有工夫流一场体面的眼泪。农庄还在那里；总得有人照料。疾病、死亡、悲恸——菜园总得照料，蔬菜总得拔起来，运到集市上，卖掉。男孩的前途，和她自己的前途，都系在这片菜园上。

===Original===
For the first few days following the funeral one or another of the neighbouring farmers drove the DeJong team to market, aided the blundering Jan in the fields. But each had his hands full with his own farm work. On the fifth day Jan Steen had to take the garden truck to Chicago, though not without many misgivings on Selina’s part, all of which were realized when he returned late next day with half the load still on his wagon and a sum of money representing exactly zero in profits. The wilted leftover vegetables were dumped behind the barn to be used later as fertilizer.
===Chinese===
葬礼后的头几天，邻近的农户这家那家轮流替德容家赶车进城赶集，到田里帮衬笨手笨脚的扬。可是各家自己的农活已经够满手满脚的了。到第五天，只好由扬·斯汀拉着园里的菜上芝加哥去，塞莉娜固然千担心万担心，而她的担心也一一应了验：第二天他很晚才回来，车上还剩着半车菜没卖掉，卖得的钱折成利润不多不少恰好是零。蔫头耷脑的剩菜倒在谷仓后面，留着日后当肥料使。

===Original===
“I didn’t do so good this time,” Jan explained, “on account I didn’t get no right place in the market.”
===Chinese===
「这一趟俺干得不大好，」扬解释道，「因为俺在集市上没占到好位子。」

===Original===
“You started early enough.”
===Chinese===
「你动身得够早的了。」

===Original===
“Well, they kind of crowded me out, like. They see I was a new hand and time I got the animals stabled and come back they had the wagon crowded out, like.”
===Chinese===
「唉，他们算是一伙儿把俺挤兑出去的。他们瞧俺是个生手，等俺把牲口安顿进棚再回来，俺那车就给他们挤到外头去了，可不。」

===Original===
Selina was standing in the kitchen doorway, Jan in the yard with the team. She turned her face toward the fields. An observant person (Jan Steen was not one of these) would have noted the singularly determined and clear-cut jaw-line of this drably calicoed farm woman.
===Chinese===
塞莉娜站在厨房门口，扬在院子里，同那挂牲口在一处。她把脸转向田里。但凡有点眼力的人（扬·斯汀不是这样的人），都会留意到这个穿一身灰扑扑印花布的农妇，那条下颌线出奇地坚决、分明。

===Original===
“I’ll go myself Monday.”
===Chinese===
「星期一我自己去。」

===Original===
Jan stared. “Go? Go where, Monday?”
===Chinese===
扬瞪大了眼。「去？去哪儿，星期一？」

===Original===
“To market.”
===Chinese===
「赶集去。」

===Original===
At this seeming pleasantry Jan Steen smiled uncertainly, shrugged his shoulders, and was off to the barn. She was always saying things that didn’t make sense. His horror and unbelief were shared by the rest of High Prairie when on Monday Selina literally took the reins in her own slim work-scarred hands.
===Chinese===
听着这句像是玩笑的话，扬·斯汀勉强笑了笑，耸耸肩膀，上谷仓去了。她净说些不着边际的话，他早惯了。到了星期一，塞莉娜当真把缰绳握进自己那双纤细而结着劳痕的手里时，他的那份骇然与不敢置信，便成了整个高草原共同的心情。

===Original===
“To market!” argued Jan as excitedly as his phlegmatic nature would permit. “A woman she don’t go to market. A woman﻿—”
===Chinese===
「赶集！」扬争辩着，激动到他那冷淡性子所能容许的限度。「女人家是不兴去赶集的。女人家——」

===Original===
“This woman does.” Selina had risen at three in the morning. Not only that, she had got Jan up, grumbling. Dirk had joined them in the fields at five. Together the three of them had pulled and bunched a wagon load. “Size them,” Selina ordered, as they started to bunch radishes, beets, turnips, carrots. “And don’t leave them loose like that. Tie them tight at the heads, like this. Twice around with the string, and through. Make bouquets of them, not bunches. And we’re going to scrub them.”
===Chinese===
「这个女人家就去。」塞莉娜凌晨三点就起了床。不止如此，她还把扬从被窝里拽起来，惹他一路嘀咕。五点钟德克也下地来跟他们会合。娘儿仨一起拔菜捆菜，装了满满一车。「按大小拣开，」开始捆萝卜、甜菜、芜菁、胡萝卜的时候，塞莉娜下着命令，「别那样松松垮垮地撂着。头部扎紧，像这样。绳子绕两圈，穿过去。把它们扎成花束，不是菜把儿。咱们还要把它们洗干净。」

===Original===
High Prairie washed its vegetables desultorily; sometimes not at all. Higgledy piggledy, large and small, they were bunched and sold as vegetables, not objets d’art. Generally there was a tan crust of good earth coating them which the housewife could scrub off at her own kitchen sink. What else had housewives to do!
===Chinese===
高草原洗菜是有一搭没一搭的；有时干脆不洗。大大小小胡乱一捆，当蔬菜卖掉，不是当艺术品卖。菜上往往还裹着一层好土结成的黄褐硬壳，主妇们尽可以回到自家厨房水池边去慢慢刷。不然主妇们还有什么可干呢！

===Original===
Selina, scrubbing the carrots vigorously under the pump, thought they emerged from their unaccustomed bath looking like clustered spears of pure gold. She knew better, though, than to say this in Jan’s hearing. Jan, by now, was sullen with bewilderment. He refused to believe that she actually intended to carry out her plan. A woman﻿—a High Prairie farmer’s wife﻿—driving to market like a man! Alone at night in the market place﻿—or at best in one of the cheap rooming houses! By Sunday somehow, mysteriously, the news had filtered through the district. High Prairie attended the Dutch Reformed church with a question hot on its tongue and Selina did not attend the morning services. A fine state of things, and she a widow of a week! High Prairie called at the DeJong farm on Sunday afternoon and was told that the widow was over in the wet west sixteen, poking about with the boy Dirk at her heels.
===Chinese===
塞莉娜在抽水泵下使劲刷着胡萝卜，觉得它们经了这场不习惯的沐浴，出来时活像一丛丛纯金打成的枪头。不过她心里有数，这话可不能让扬听见。这时扬已经被惶惑憋出了一脸阴沉。他死活不肯相信她当真要照她的主意干。一个女人家——高草原庄稼人的老婆——像个男人一样赶车去赶集！夜里孤零零守在集市上——顶多也就是住进哪家廉价宿舍！不知怎么地，神不知鬼不觉地，到星期天这消息已经渗遍了全区。高草原人上荷兰归正会做礼拜，舌尖上烫着同一个问题，而塞莉娜偏偏没来赴早祷。真是一幅好光景，她可是个死了男人才一星期的寡妇！星期天下午，高草原人登门拜访德容家农庄，得到的回话是：寡妇在西边那片湿漉漉的十六英亩里，东戳戳西翻翻，男孩德克跟在她脚跟后头。

===Original===
The Reverend Dekker appeared late Sunday afternoon on his way to evening service. A dour dominie, the Reverend Dekker, and one whose talents were anachronistic. He would have been invaluable in the days when New York was New Amsterdam. But the second and third generations of High Prairie Dutch were beginning to chafe under his old-world regime. A hard blue eye, had the Reverend Dekker, and a fanatic one.
===Chinese===
德克尔牧师在星期天傍晚露了面，正赶着去晚祷。德克尔牧师是个阴郁执拗的旧派教士（dominie），一身本事生不逢辰。在纽约还叫新阿姆斯特丹的年代，他本该是无价之宝。可是高草原荷兰人的第二代、第三代，已经开始在他那套旧世界的辖制下渐渐磨出反抗之心。德克尔牧师生着一双铁青的眼睛，狂信徒的眼睛。

===Original===
“What is this talk I hear, Mrs. DeJong, that you are going to the Haymarket with the garden stuff, a woman alone?”
===Chinese===
「德容太太，我听到的那些话是怎么回事？说你一个女流之辈，要独自拉着园里的菜上海马克特去？」

===Original===
“Dirk goes with me.”
===Chinese===
「德克跟我一起去。」

===Original===
“You don’t know what you are doing, Mrs. DeJong. The Haymarket is no place for a decent woman. As for the boy! There is card-playing, drinking﻿—all manner of wickedness﻿—daughters of Jezebel on the street, going among the wagons.”
===Chinese===
「你不知道自己在做的是什么，德容太太。海马克特可不是正经女人去的地方。至于那孩子！那里有玩牌的，喝酒的——各式各样的邪行——街上还有耶洗别的女儿们，在车队中间钻来钻去。」

===Original===
“Really!” said Selina. It sounded thrilling, after twelve years on the farm.
===Chinese===
「真的吗！」塞莉娜说。在农庄上待了十二年，这话听起来怪带劲的。

===Original===
“You must not go.”
===Chinese===
「你不可去。」

===Original===
“The vegetables are rotting in the ground. And Dirk and I must live.”
===Chinese===
「菜在地里都要烂了。我和德克总得活命。」

===Original===
“Remember the two sparrows. ‘One of them shall not fall on the ground without’ —﻿Matthew 10:29.”
===Chinese===
「想想那两只麻雀。『若不是天父允许，一只也必不落在地上』——马太福音10:29。」

===Original===
“I don’t see,” replied Selina, simply, “what good that does the sparrow, once it’s fallen.”
===Chinese===
「我不明白，」塞莉娜简单地答道，「麻雀已经掉在地上了，这话对它还有什么好处。」

===Original===
By Monday afternoon the parlour curtains of every High Prairie farmhouse that faced the Halsted road were agitated as though by a brisk wind between the hours of three and five, when the market wagons were to be seen moving toward Chicago. Klaas Pool at dinner that noon had spoken of Selina’s contemplated trip with a mingling of pity and disapproval.
===Chinese===
到了星期一下午，三点到五点之间，凡是朝向哈尔斯特德路的高草原农舍，客厅的窗帘都骚动起来，仿佛被一阵疾风吹拂——正是赶集的马车纷纷驶往芝加哥的时辰。那天中午克拉斯·普尔在饭桌上说起塞莉娜筹划中的这趟行程，语气里怜悯与非难兼而有之。

===Original===
“It ain’t decent a woman should drive to market.”
===Chinese===
「女人家赶车去赶集，不成体统。」

===Original===
Mrs. Klaas Pool (they still spoke of her as the Widow Paarlenberg) smiled her slippery crooked smile. “What could you expect! Look how she’s always acted.”
===Chinese===
克拉斯·普尔太太（人们提起她，仍旧叫她帕伦贝格寡妇）咧开她那抹狡猾的歪嘴笑。「还能指望什么！瞧她平日那副做派。」

===Original===
Klaas did not follow this. He was busy with his own train of thought. “It don’t seem hardly possible. Time she come here school teacher I drove her out and she was like a little robin or what, set up on the seat. She says, I remember like yesterday, cabbages was beautiful. I bet she learned different by this time.”
===Chinese===
克拉斯没接这个茬。他正忙着琢磨自己的心事。「简直叫人不敢信。她当女教师刚来那阵，是我赶车接她来的，她坐在车座上，活像只小知更鸟什么的。她说——我记到今天，跟昨天的事一样——甘蓝菜好看得很。我敢打赌，这会儿她保管学乖了。」

===Original===
But she hadn’t. So little had Selina learned in these past eleven years that now, having loaded the wagon in the yard she surveyed it with more sparkle in her eye than High Prairie would have approved in a widow of little more than a week. They had picked and bunched only the best of the late crop﻿—the firmest reddest radishes, the roundest juiciest beets; the carrots that tapered a good seven inches from base to tip; kraut cabbages of the drumhead variety that were flawless green balls; firm juicy spears of cucumber; cauliflower (of her own planting; Pervus had opposed it) that looked like a bride’s bouquet. Selina stepped back now and regarded this riot of crimson and green, of white and gold and purple.
===Chinese===
可她没有学乖。过去这十一年，塞莉娜学到的东西竟如此之少，以至于此刻她在院子里装好一车菜，眼里的光彩比高草原人容得下一个死了男人刚过一星期的寡妇所该有的还要多。他们只拣晚茬里最好的摘了、捆了——最结实最红的萝卜，最圆最水灵的甜菜；胡萝卜从根到梢足有七英寸，一路匀称地收细；扁圆种的腌菜甘蓝，个个是无瑕的绿球；黄瓜条条坚挺多汁；菜花（她亲手种的；当初珀维斯还反对来着）活像一束新娘捧花。塞莉娜退后一步，端详着这一车绯红与翠绿、雪白与金黄与绛紫的烂漫。

===Original===
“Aren’t they beautiful! Dirk, aren’t they beautiful!”
===Chinese===
「它们多好看啊！德克，它们多好看啊！」

===Original===
Dirk, capering in his excitement at the prospect of the trip before him, shook his head impatiently. “What? I don’t see anything beautiful. What’s beautiful?”
===Chinese===
德克想到眼前这趟出行，兴奋得直蹦跶，此刻却不耐烦地摇了摇头。「什么？我看不出有什么好看的。什么好看？」

===Original===
Selina flung out her arms. “The﻿—the whole wagon load. The cabbages.”
===Chinese===
塞莉娜张开双臂。「这——这一整车呀。这甘蓝。」

===Original===
“I don’t know what you mean,” said Dirk. “Let’s go, Mother. Aren’t we going now? You said as soon as the load was on.”
===Chinese===
「我不懂你什么意思，」德克说。「走吧，妈妈。咱们这不是要走了吗？你说了装完车就走的。」

===Original===
“Oh, Sobig, you’re just exactly like your﻿—” She stopped.
===Chinese===
「唉，『这么大』，你呀，真是活脱脱像你的——」她顿住了。

===Original===
“Like my what?”
===Chinese===
「像我的什么？」

===Original===
“We’ll go now, son. There’s cold meat for your supper, Jan, and potatoes all sliced for frying and half an apple pie left from noon. Wash your dishes﻿—don’t leave them cluttering around the kitchen.
