You ought to get in the rest of the squash and pumpkins by evening. Maybe I can sell the lot instead of taking them in by the load. I’ll see a commission man. Take less, if I have to.”
===Chinese===
「这就走，孩子。扬，你的晚饭有冷肉，土豆都切好片了预备煎，还有中午剩下的半块苹果派。碗要洗——别堆得厨房里到处都是。傍晚前你该把剩下的笋瓜和南瓜都收进来。这一批说不定我能整批卖掉，不用按车交给菜行。我去找个代卖商。价钱低就低些，不得已嘛。」

===Original===
She had dressed the boy in his homemade suit cut down from one of his father’s. He wore a wide-brimmed straw hat which he hated. Selina had made him an overcoat of stout bean-sacking and this she tucked under the wagon seat, together with an old black fascinator, for though the September afternoon was white-hot she knew that the evenings were likely to be chilly, once the sun, a great crimson Chinese balloon, had burned itself out in a blaze of flame across the prairie horizon. Selina herself, in a full-skirted black-stuff dress, mounted the wagon agilely, took up the reins, looked down at the boy seated beside her, clucked to the horses. Jan Steen gave vent to a final outraged bellow.
===Chinese===
她给男孩穿上一身改自他父亲旧衣的家制衣裳。他戴一顶宽边草帽，恨得牙痒。塞莉娜还用结实的豆袋布给他做了件外套，此刻连同一条旧的黑色绒线头巾（fascinator）一起塞进了车座底下——虽说九月的下午白炽般毒热，她可知道一到傍晚多半要凉下来，等那太阳，那巨大的绯红中国气球，在草原天际线上烧尽最后一团火焰熄灭之后。塞莉娜自己穿着一条宽摆的黑布裙，敏捷地登上车，拾起缰绳，低头看了看坐在身边的男孩，咂嘴催马。扬·斯汀发出最后一嗓子怒气冲天的吼叫。

===Original===
“Never in my life did I hear of such a thing!”
===Chinese===
「活这么大，就没听说过这种事！」

===Original===
Selina turned the horses’ heads toward the city. “You’d be surprised, Jan, to know of all the things you’re going to hear of some day that you’ve never heard of before.” Still, when twenty years had passed and the Ford, the phonograph, the radio, and the rural mail delivery had dumped the world at Jan’s plodding feet he liked to tell of that momentous day when Selina DeJong had driven off to market like a man with a wagon load of hand-scrubbed garden truck and the boy Dirk perched beside her on the seat.
===Chinese===
塞莉娜拨转马头朝城里去。「扬，往后你会听见许多如今从没听说过的事，到时候你自己都会吃惊的。」不过，等到二十年过去，福特车、留声机、无线电和乡村邮递把整个世界倒在扬那双磨磨蹭蹭的脚前时，他倒喜欢跟人讲起那个重大的日子：塞莉娜·德容像个男人一样赶车去赶集，拉着一车亲手刷洗的园菜，男孩德克高踞在她身旁的车座上。

===Original===
If, then, you had been travelling the Halsted road, you would have seen a decrepit wagon, vegetable-laden, driven by a too-thin woman, sallow, bright-eyed, in a shapeless black dress, a battered black felt hat that looked like a man’s old “fedora” and probably was. Her hair was unbecomingly strained away from the face with its high cheek bones, so that unless you were really observant you failed to notice the exquisite little nose or the really fine eyes so unnaturally large now in the anxious face. On the seat beside her you would have seen a farm boy of nine or thereabouts﻿—a brown freckle-faced lad in a comically homemade suit of clothes and a straw hat with a broken and flopping brim which he was forever jerking off only to have it set firmly on again by the woman who seemed to fear the effects of the hot afternoon sun on his close-cropped head. But in the brief intervals when the hat was off you must have noted how the boy’s eyes were shining.
===Chinese===
倘若那时你正走在哈尔斯特德路上，你会看见一辆破破烂烂的货车，满载蔬菜，赶车的是个瘦过头的女人，面色萎黄，眼睛发亮，穿一身没模样的黑衣裳，戴一顶压瘪的黑毡帽，看着像男人戴旧的「软呢帽」（fedora），多半本来就是。她的头发紧绷绷地拢离那张颧骨高高的脸，实在不衬，以至于你若不是格外留心，便会错过那只精巧的小鼻子，错过那双真正美的眼睛——在那张焦灼的脸上，它们此刻显得大得出奇。在她身旁的车座上，你会看见一个九岁上下的农家男孩——褐色皮肤、满脸雀斑的小子，穿着一身滑稽的家制衣裳，戴一顶断了圈、耷拉着边的草帽，总在一把一把往下扯，又总被那女人结结实实给扣回去——她似乎生怕午后的毒日头晒坏了他那剪得短短的头。可就在帽子摘下来的那几个短短的当口，你准会注意到，这孩子的眼睛多么亮。

===Original===
At their feet was the dog Pom, a mongrel whose tail bore no relation to his head, whose ill-assorted legs appeared wholly at variance with his sturdy barrel of a body. He dozed now, for it had been his duty to watch the wagon load at night, while Pervus slept.
===Chinese===
他们脚边卧着狗波姆（Pom），一条杂种狗，尾巴和脑袋全不搭调，那四条长短不齐的腿，看着也和它那圆桶般结实的身子全不相干。这会儿它正打盹——夜里看守一车菜本是它的差事，趁珀维斯睡觉的时候。

===Original===
A shabby enough little outfit, but magnificent, too. Here was Selina DeJong driving up the Halsted road toward the city instead of sitting, black-robed, in the farm parlour while High Prairie came to condole. In Selina, as they jogged along the hot dusty way, there welled up a feeling very like elation. Conscious of this, the New England strain in her took her to task. “Selina Peake, aren’t you ashamed of yourself! You’re a wicked woman! Feeling almost gay when you ought to be sad.﻿ ﻿… Poor Pervus﻿ ﻿… the farm﻿ ﻿… Dirk﻿ ﻿… and you can feel almost gay! You ought to be ashamed of yourself!”
===Chinese===
好一副寒酸的小行头，却也气派。瞧，塞莉娜·德容正沿着哈尔斯特德路朝城里去，而不是一身黑衣坐在农庄客厅里，任高草原人登门吊慰。一路颠簸在滚烫扬尘的路上，塞莉娜心头涌起一种近乎欢欣的感觉。察觉到这一点，她体内的新英格兰血统便来责问她。「塞莉娜·皮克，你不害臊吗！你是个坏女人！该悲伤的时候，你倒几乎快活起来。……可怜的珀维斯……这农庄……德克……你居然还几乎快活！你该害臊才是！」

===Original===
But she wasn’t, and knew it. For even as she thought this the little wave of elation came flooding over her again. More than ten years ago she had driven with Klaas Pool up that same road for the first time, and in spite of the recent tragedy of her father’s death, her youth, her loneliness, the terrifying thought of the new home to which she was going, a stranger among strangers, she had been conscious of a warm little thrill of elation, of excitement﻿—of adventure! That was it. “The whole thing’s just a grand adventure,” her father, Simeon Peake, had said. And now the sensations of that day were repeating themselves. Now, as then, she was doing what was considered a revolutionary and daring thing; a thing that High Prairie regarded with horror. And now, as then, she took stock. Youth was gone, but she had health, courage; a boy of nine; twenty-five acres of worn-out farm land; dwelling and outhouses in a bad state of repair; and a gay adventuresome spirit that was never to die, though it led her into curious places and she often found, at the end, only a trackless waste from which she had to retrace her steps painfully. But always, to her, red and green cabbages were to be jade and burgundy, chrysoprase and porphyry. Life has no weapons against a woman like that.
===Chinese===
可她并不害臊，她自己知道。因为就在这样想的时候，那阵小小的欢欣又漫上她的心头。十多年前，她跟着克拉斯·普尔头一回走上这条路，尽管新遭丧父之痛，尽管年少，孤单，一想到要去一个举目无亲的陌生人家便心惊胆战，她当时也分明觉出过一阵暖烘烘的战栗，一阵兴奋——一场奇遇！正是这个。「整个这件事，不过是一场盛大的奇遇，」她父亲西缅·皮克当年这样说过。此刻，那一天的感觉又重演了。同当年一样，她正在做一件世人眼中的惊世骇俗、胆大包天的事；一件高草原人视为骇人的事。同当年一样，她盘点起自己的家底。青春没有了，可她有健康，有胆气；一个九岁的男孩；二十五英亩种乏了的田；一座失修得一塌糊涂的正屋和棚舍；还有一颗至死不泯的快活冒险的心——虽说这颗心常把她领进奇奇怪怪的去处，而她往往到了尽头才发现，那里只有一片无路的荒野，害她一步一步痛楚地折返。可是对她来说，红的绿的甘蓝，永远会是玉和勃艮第，是绿玉髓和斑岩。生活对这样的女人，是拿不出武器来的。

===Original===
And the wine-red cashmere. She laughed aloud.
===Chinese===
还有那件酒红色开司米。她放声笑了。

===Original===
“What are you laughing at, Mom?”
===Chinese===
「你笑什么呀，妈？」

===Original===
That sobered her. “Oh, nothing, Sobig. I didn’t know I was laughing. I was just thinking about a red dress I had when I first came to High Prairie a girl. I’ve got it yet.”
===Chinese===
她一下子敛了笑。「哦，没什么，『这么大』。我不知道自己在笑。我是想起一条红裙子，我还是个姑娘家、刚到高草原那年穿的。它还在我手里呢。」

===Original===
“What’s that to laugh at?” He was following a yellowhammer with his eyes.
===Chinese===
「那有什么好笑的？」他正拿眼睛追着一只黄鹀（yellowhammer）。

===Original===
“Nothing. Mother said it was nothing.”
===Chinese===
「没什么。我母亲当年就说，这没什么。」

===Original===
“Wisht I’d brought my slingshot.” The yellowhammer was perched on the fence by the roadside not ten feet away.
===Chinese===
「真该把弹弓带来。」那只黄鹀就栖在路边栅栏上，离着不到十英尺。

===Original===
“Sobig, you promised me you wouldn’t throw at any more birds, ever.”
===Chinese===
「『这么大』，你答应过我的，再也不拿弹弓打鸟了，永远不。」

===Original===
“Oh, I wouldn’t hit it. I would just like to aim at it.”
===Chinese===
「哦，我不会打中的。我就是想拿它瞄一瞄准。」

===Original===
Down the hot dusty country road. She was serious enough now. The cost of the funeral to be paid. The doctor’s bills. Jan’s wage. All the expenses, large and small, of the poor little farm holding. Nothing to laugh at, certainly. The boy was wiser than she.
===Chinese===
顺着滚烫扬尘的乡路走下去。这会儿她够郑重了。丧葬费要付。医生的账单。扬的工钱。这份可怜的小田产里里外外、大大小小的一切开销。确实没什么好笑的。这孩子比她有见识。

===Original===
“There’s Mrs. Pool on her porch, Mom. Rocking.”
===Chinese===
「妈，普尔太太在自家游廊上呢。摇着椅子。」

===Original===
There, indeed, was the erstwhile Widow Paarlenberg on her porch, rocking. A pleasant place to be in mid-afternoon of a hot September day. She stared at the creaking farm wagon, vegetable laden; at the boy perched on the high seat; at the sallow shabby woman who was charioteer for the whole crazy outfit. Mrs. Klaas Pool’s pink face creased in a smile. She sat forward in her chair and ceased to rock.
===Chinese===
可不是嘛，昔日的那位帕伦贝格寡妇果然在自家游廊上，摇着椅子。九月热天的午后三点钟，坐在那儿倒是挺受用的。她盯着那辆吱吱呀呀、满载蔬菜的农家货车；盯着高踞在车座上的男孩；盯着那个面色萎黄、衣衫寒酸、给这一整套疯疯癫癫行头执辔的女人。克拉斯·普尔太太那张粉脸绽出笑纹。她在椅子上坐直了身子，椅子也不摇了。

===Original===
“Where you going this hot day, Mis’ DeJong?”
===Chinese===
「这么热的天，德容太太，你这是上哪儿去呀？」

===Original===
Selina sat up very straight. “To Baghdad, Mrs. Pool.”
===Chinese===
塞莉娜把腰背挺得笔直。「上巴格达去呀，普尔太太。」

===Original===
“To﻿—Where’s that? What for?”
===Chinese===
「上——那是什么地方？去干什么？」

===Original===
“To sell my jewels, Mrs. Pool. And to see Aladdin, and Harun-al-Rashid and Ali Baba. And the Forty Thieves.”
===Chinese===
「去卖我的珠宝呀，普尔太太。还要去看阿拉丁，看哈伦·拉希德（Harun-al-Rashid），看阿里巴巴。还有那四十大盗。」

===Original===
Mrs. Pool had left her rocker and had come down the steps. The wagon creaked on past her gate. She took a step or two down the path, and called after them. “I never heard of it. Bag﻿—How do you get there?”
===Chinese===
普尔太太已经离了摇椅，走下台阶。货车吱呀吱呀赶过了她家院门。她顺着小径又追出一两步，朝他们背影喊。「我可没听说过。巴格——那地方怎么走啊？」

===Original===
Over her shoulder Selina called out from the wagon seat. “You just go until you come to a closed door. And you say ‘Open Sesame!’ and there you are.”
===Chinese===
塞莉娜从车座上回过头来喊。「你只管往前走，走到一扇关着的门跟前。你说一声『芝麻开门！』（Open Sesame），就到啦。」

===Original===
Bewilderment shadowed Mrs. Pool’s placid face. As the wagon lurched on down the road it was Selina who was smiling and Mrs. Pool who was serious.
===Chinese===
惶惑给普尔太太那张恬静的脸罩上了一层阴影。货车一路颠着朝前去了，笑的是塞莉娜，郑重其事的是普尔太太。

===Original===
The boy, round eyed, was looking up at his mother. “That’s out of *Arabian Nights*, what you said. Why did you say that?” Suddenly excitement tinged his voice. “That’s out of the book. Isn’t it? Isn’t it! We’re not really﻿—”
===Chinese===
男孩睁圆了眼睛仰头看母亲。「你刚说的那些，是《一千零一夜》（*Arabian Nights*）里的。你为什么说那个？」忽然间他的嗓音里透出兴奋。「那是书里头的。是不是？是不是！咱们并不是真的——」

===Original===
She was a little contrite, but not very. “Well, not really, perhaps. But ’most any place is Baghdad if you don’t know what will happen in it. And this is an adventure, isn’t it, that we’re going on? How can you tell! All kind of things can happen. All kinds of people. People in disguise in the Haymarket. Caliphs, and princes, and slaves, and thieves, and good fairies, and witches.”
===Chinese===
她有点过意不去，但也不大有。「呃，也许不是真的。可只要你还不知道一个地方会发生什么，那它差不多就是巴格达。再说咱们这是去历险呢，不是吗？你怎么晓得！什么样的事都可能碰上。什么样的人都可能遇见。海马克特里有乔装改扮的人。哈里发，王子，奴隶，强盗，善心的仙女，还有巫婆。」

===Original===
“In the Haymarket! That Pop went to all the time! That is just dumb talk.”
===Chinese===
「在海马克特！爸从前不是老去嘛！你这是净说傻话。」

===Original===
Within Selina something cried out, “Don’t say that, Sobig! Don’t say that!”
===Chinese===
塞莉娜心里有什么在喊，「别说这话，『这么大』！别说这话！」

===Original===
On down the road. Here a head at a front room window. There a woman’s calicoed figure standing in the doorway. Mrs. Vander Sijde on the porch, fanning her flushed face with her apron; Cornelia Snip in the yard pretending to tie up the drooping stalks of the golden-glow and eyeing the approaching team with the avid gossip’s gaze. To these Selina waved, bowed, called.
===Chinese===
一路走下去。这里，一扇临街窗后有个人影探出头。那里，一个穿印花布的女人立在门口。范德赛德太太在游廊上，用围裙扇着通红的脸；科妮莉亚·斯尼普（Cornelia Snip）在院子里，假装在捆那丛垂了头的金光菊（golden-glow），一双包打听的馋眼睛却盯住越走越近的车马。对这些人，塞莉娜挥手、点头、打招呼。

===Original===
“How d’you do, Mrs. Vander Sijde!”
===Chinese===
「您好啊，范德赛德太太！」

===Original===
A prim reply to this salutation. Disapproval writ large on the farm-wife’s flushed face.
===Chinese===
对这句招呼，回过来一句一本正经的答话。不以为然四个大字写在那农妇通红的脸上。

===Original===
“Hello, Cornelia!”
===Chinese===
「你好啊，科妮莉亚！」

===Original===
A pretended start, notable for its bad acting. “Oh, is it you, Mrs. DeJong! Sun’s in my eyes. I couldn’t think it was you like that.”
===Chinese===
一声做出来的惊呼，演技坏得刺眼。「哟，是德容太太呀！太阳晃我的眼。那样儿我可没想到是你。」

===Original===
Women’s eyes, hostile, cold, peering.
===Chinese===
女人们的眼睛，怀着的，冷冷的，窥探着。

===Original===
Five o’clock. Six. The boy climbed over the wheel, filled a tin pail with water at a farmhouse well. They ate and drank as they rode along, for there was no time to lose. Bread and meat and pickles and pie. There were vegetables in the wagon, ripe for eating. There were other varieties that Selina might have cooked at home in preparation for this meal﻿—German celery root boiled tender and soaked in vinegar; red beets, pickled; onions; coleslaw; beans. They would have regarded these with an apathetic eye all too familiar with the sight of them. Selina knew now why the Pools’ table, in her schoolteacher days, had been so lacking in the green stuff she had craved. The thought of cooking the spinach which she had planted, weeded, spaded, tended, picked, washed, bunched, filled her with a nausea of distaste such as she might have experienced at the contemplation of cannibalism.
===Chinese===
五点了。六点了。男孩攀过车轮，在一户农家的井台打满一马口铁桶水。他们边赶路边吃边喝，因为一刻工夫也耽搁不起。面包，冷肉，腌菜，馅饼。车上就有蔬菜，现成能吃的。还有些品种，塞莉娜本可以在家做好了带着当这顿饭的——德国根芹菜（celery root）煮得烂熟再用醋泡；红甜菜腌的；洋葱；凉拌卷心菜丝；豆子。可他们见了这些只会眼皮都懒得抬——实在看熟看腻了。塞莉娜如今才明白，她当女教师那阵子，普尔家的饭桌上为什么那样缺绿菜，缺她馋的那些。一想到要下厨做那菠菜——她种的、锄的、翻的、侍弄的、摘的、洗的、捆的——她就一阵反胃般的厌腻，好比想到人吃人会起的那阵恶心。

===Original===
The boy had started out bravely enough in the heat of the day, sitting up very straight beside his mother, calling to the horses, shrieking and waving his arms at chickens that flew squawking across the road. Now he began to droop. Evening was coming on. A cool blanket of air from the lake on the east enveloped them with the suddenness characteristic of the region, and the mist began to drift across the prairie, softening the autumn stubble, cooling the dusty road, misting the parched willows by the roadside, hazing the shabby squat farmhouses.
===Chinese===
大晌午出发时，这男孩还挺精神，笔直地坐在母亲身旁，吆喝牲口，朝着咯咯尖叫着飞过路面的鸡群又喊又叫抡胳膊。这会儿他开始蔫了。傍晚来了。东边湖上飘来一层凉气，像毯子似的裹住他们——来得那样骤然，正是这地方特有的脾气——雾开始漫过草原，柔化了秋天的茬地，凉了扬尘的大路，润了路边焦渴的柳树，朦胧了那些低矮寒酸的农舍。

===Original===
She brushed away the crumbs, packed the remaining bread and meat thriftily into the basket and covered it with a napkin against the boy’s future hunger should he waken in the night.
===Chinese===
她掸去面包屑，把剩下的面包和冷肉仔细装回篮子里，拿餐巾盖好，预备男孩夜里醒了饿。

===Original===
“Sleepy, Sobig?”
===Chinese===
「困了，『这么大』？」

===Original===
“No. Should say not.” His lids were heavy. His face and body, relaxed, took on the soft baby contours that come with weariness. The sun was low. Sunset gloried the west in a final flare of orange and crimson. Dusk. The boy drooped against her heavy, sagging. She wrapped the old black fascinator about him. He opened his eyes, tugged at the wrapping about his shoulders. “Don’t want the old thing﻿ ﻿… fas’nator﻿ ﻿… like a girl﻿ ﻿…” drooped again with a sigh and found the soft curve where her side just cushioned his head. In the twilight the dust gleamed white on weeds, and brush, and grass. The far-off mellow sonance of a cowbell. Horses’ hoofs clopping up behind them, a wagon passing in a cloud of dust, a curious backward glance, or a greeting exchanged.
===Chinese===
「不困。才不呢。」眼皮却沉了。脸和身子一放松，便泛起倦意里才有的那种婴孩似的圆软轮廓。太阳低了。落日用最后一派橙与绯红烧亮了西天。暮色。男孩倚在她身上，沉甸甸地往下坠。她把那条旧的黑色绒线头巾给他裹上。他睁开眼，扯了扯肩上的包裹。「不要这老古董……头……头巾……跟个丫头似的……」叹口气又歪下去，寻到她腰侧那道恰能垫住他脑袋的软弯。暮色里，白尘微微发亮，落在杂草上，灌木上，草叶上。远远一声牛铃，温润悠扬。马蹄声得得地从背后赶上来，一辆车扬着尘过去了，有人回头好奇地望一眼，或者互道一声好。

===Original===
One of the Ooms boys, or Jakob Boomsma. “You’re never going to market, Mis’ DeJong!” staring with china-blue eyes at her load.
===Chinese===
奥姆斯家的小子，要不就是雅各布·布姆斯马（Jakob Boomsma）。「德容太太，你总不会是去赶集吧！」淡瓷蓝色的眼睛直勾勾盯着她那一车菜。

===Original===
“Yes, I am, Mr. Boomsma.”
===Chinese===
「正是去赶集呢，布姆斯马先生。」

===Original===
“That ain’t work for a woman, Mis’ DeJong. You better stay home and let the men folks go.”
===Chinese===
「那可不是女人家干的活，德容太太。你还是待在家里，让爷们儿去的好。」

===Original===
Selina’s men folks looked up at her﻿—one with the asking eyes of a child, one with the trusting eyes of a dog. “My men folks are going,” answered Selina. But then, they had always thought her a little queer, so it didn’t matter much.
===Chinese===
塞莉娜的「爷们儿」仰头望着她——一双是孩子般发问的眼睛，一双是狗般信赖的眼睛。「我家的爷们儿这就去呢。」塞莉娜答道。不过话说回来，这些人本来就一向当她有点古怪，所以也无关紧要。

===Original===
She urged the horses on, refusing to confess to herself her dread of the destination which they were approaching. Lights now, in the houses along the way, and those houses closer together. She wrapped the reins around the whip, and holding the sleeping boy with one hand reached beneath the seat with the other for the coat of sacking. This she placed around him snugly, folded an empty sack for a pillow, and lifting the boy in her arms laid him gently on the lumpy bed formed by the bags of potatoes piled up just behind the seat in the back of the wagon. So the boy slept. Night had come on.
===Chinese===
她催着马往前赶，不肯对自己承认，她正怕着他们一步步挨近的那个目的地。沿路的窗子里有灯了，房子也越挨越密。她把缰绳缠在鞭子上，一只手搂着睡熟的男孩，另一只手探到座位底下，去抽那件麻袋布外套。她把它给他裹严实，又折了一只空口袋当枕头，然后把男孩抱起来，轻轻放在车座后面那堆土豆袋铺成的高低不平的床上。男孩就这样睡了。夜来了。

===Original===
The figure of the woman drooped a little now as the old wagon creaked on toward Chicago. A very small figure in the black dress and a shawl over her shoulders. She had taken off her old black felt hat. The breeze ruffled her hair that was fine and soft, and it made a little halo about the white face that gleamed almost luminously in the darkness as she turned it up toward the sky.
===Chinese===
老货车吱呀吱呀地朝芝加哥赶着路，那女人的身形也微微佝偻了些。黑裙上披一条披肩，一个小小的身影。她已摘下那顶旧黑毡帽。微风吹乱她细软的头发，给那张仰向夜空的白脸围出一圈小小的光晕——那张脸在黑暗里几乎莹莹发亮。

===Original===
“I’ll sleep out with Sobig in the wagon. It won’t hurt either of us. It will be warm in town, there in the Haymarket. Twenty-five cents﻿—maybe fifty for the two of us, in the rooming house. Fifty cents just to sleep. It takes hours of work in the fields to make fifty cents.”
===Chinese===
「我就陪『这么大』睡在车上。不碍着咱们俩的事。城里头暖和，海马克特那儿。两毛五——娘儿俩住宿舍兴许五毛。光是睡一觉就五毛。地里刨挣出五毛钱来，得搭上好几个钟头的活。」

===Original===
She was sleepy now. The night air was deliciously soft and soothing. In her nostrils was the smell of the fields, of grass dew-wet, of damp dust, of cattle; the pungent prick of goldenrod, and occasionally a scented wave that meant wild phlox in a nearby ditch. She sniffed all this gratefully, her mind and body curiously alert to sounds, scents, forms even, in the darkness. She had suffered much in the past week; had eaten and slept but little. Had known terror, bewilderment, agony, shock. Now she was relaxed, receptive, a little lightheaded perhaps, what with under-feeding and tears and overwork. The racking process had cleared brain and bowels; had washed her spiritually clean; had quickened her perceptions abnormally. Now she was like a delicate and sensitive electric instrument keyed to receive and register; vibrating to every ether wave.
===Chinese===
这会儿她困了。夜气软得沁人，熨帖极了。她鼻孔里是田野的气味，带露的草，潮乎乎的尘土，牲口；一枝黄花（goldenrod）那股冲鼻的辛辣，偶尔还掠过一鼻子的香，那是附近沟里的野生福禄考（phlox）。她感激地嗅着这一切，心思和身体都出奇地警醒，连黑暗里的声响、气味、乃至形影都瞒不过她。过去这一星期她受够了苦；吃没好吃，睡没好睡。经了恐怖、惶惑、剧痛、震惊。此刻她松弛了，敞开了，也许还有点头轻飘飘——饿的，哭的，累的。这一番剐骨的煎熬涤清了她的头脑和脏腑；把她洗得灵里干干净净；把她的知觉磨得异样地锋利。此刻她像一台精细灵敏的电器，校准了专司接收与记录；每一道电波掠过，她便应声震颤。

===Original===
She drove along in the dark, a dowdy farm woman in shapeless garments; just a bundle on the rickety seat of a decrepit truck wagon. The boy slept on his hard lumpy bed like the little vegetable that he was. The farm lights went out. The houses were blurs in the black. The lights of the city came nearer. She was thinking clearly, if disconnectedly, without bitterness, without reproach.
===Chinese===
她在黑暗里赶着车，一个衣衫没形没样的邋遢农妇；不过是辆破菜车摇晃座上的一小捆行李。男孩在他那硬邦邦、坑坑洼洼的床上睡着，真像一棵小蔬菜。农家的灯一盏盏熄了。房屋在夜色里化成一团团模糊的黑。城市的灯火近了。她想得清清楚楚，尽管断断续续，不怨，也不恨。

===Original===
“My father was wrong. He said that life was a great adventure﻿—a fine show. He said the more things that happen to you the richer you are, even if they’re not pleasant things. That’s living, he said. No matter what happens to you, good or bad, it’s just so much﻿—what was that word he used?﻿—so much﻿—oh, yes﻿—‘velvet.’ Just so much velvet. Well, it isn’t true. He had brains, and charm, and knowledge and he died in a gambling house, shot while looking on at someone else who was to have been killed.﻿ ﻿… Now we’re on the cobblestones. Will Dirk wake up? My little So Big.﻿ ﻿… No, he’s asleep. Asleep on a pile of potato sacks because his mother thought that life was a grand adventure﻿—a fine show﻿—and that you took it as it came. A lie! I’ve taken it as it came and made the best of it. That isn’t the way. You take the best, and make the most of it﻿ ﻿… Thirty-fifth Street, that was. Another hour and a half to reach the Haymarket.﻿ ﻿… I’m not afraid. After all, you just sell your vegetables for what you can get.﻿ ﻿… Well, it’s going to be different with him. I mustn’t call him Sobig any more. He doesn’t like it. Dirk. That’s a fine name. Dirk DeJong.﻿ ﻿… No drifting along for him. I’ll see that he starts with a plan, and follows it. He’ll have every chance. Every chance. Too late for me, now, but he’ll be different.﻿ ﻿… Twenty-second Street﻿ ﻿… Twelfth﻿ ﻿… Look at all the people!﻿ ﻿… I’m enjoying this. No use denying it. I’m enjoying this. Just as I enjoyed driving along with Klaas Pool that evening, years and years ago. Scared, but enjoying it. Perhaps I oughtn’t to be﻿—but that’s hypocritical and sneaking. Why not, if I really do enjoy it! I’ll wake him.﻿ ﻿… Dirk! Dirk, we’re almost there. Look at all the people, and the lights. We’re almost there.”
===Chinese===
我父亲错了。他说人生是一场盛大的奇遇——一出好戏。他说临到你头上的事越多，你就越富有，哪怕不是愉快的事。这才叫活着，他说。不管临到你头上的是什么，好的坏的，都不过是那么些——他用的那个词是什么来着？——那么些——哦，对了——『白饶的外快』。不过是那么些白饶的外快。哼，这不是真的。他有头脑，有魅力，有见识，却死在一家赌场里，在旁观看时挨了枪——原本要杀的是另一个人。……这会儿上鹅卵石路了。德克会不会醒？我的小「这—么大」。……不，他睡着呢。睡在一堆土豆袋上，就因为他母亲当年以为人生是一场盛大的奇遇——一出好戏——以为来什么便受什么。谎话！我就是来什么受什么，还事事都往好里撑。那不是路子。要挑最好的拿，再把它使到尽……刚过去的是三十五街。到海马克特还有一个半钟头。……我不怕。说到底，蔬菜嘛，能卖几个钱就卖几个钱。……哼，到他这儿，一切都要换个样。我不能再叫他『这么大』了。他不喜欢。德克。这名字好听。德克·德容。……他可不能再这样随波逐流地漂。我要让他带着一套章法起步，并且贯彻到底。他要得到每一个机会。每一个机会。如今我这边是迟了，可他会不一样。……二十二街……十二街……瞧这一街的人！……我这是在乐呢。赖也没用。我这是在乐呢。就像多少多少年前那个傍晚，我坐在克拉斯·普尔的车上，一路乐着一样。怕，可还是乐。兴许我不该乐——可那是假惺惺，是偷偷摸摸。真乐，为什么不能乐！我去叫醒他。……德克！德克，咱们快到了。瞧这一街的人，这一街的灯。咱们快到了。

===Original===
The boy awoke, raised himself from his bed of sacking, looked about, blinked, sank back again and curled into a ball. “Don’t want to see the lights﻿ ﻿… people﻿ ﻿…”
===Chinese===
男孩醒了，从麻袋床上撑起身，四下里望了望，眨眨眼，又倒回去，蜷成一团。「不看灯……不看人……」

===Original===
He was asleep again. Selina guided the horses skilfully through the downtown streets. She looked about with wide ambient eyes. Other wagons passed her. There was a line of them ahead of her. The men looked at her curiously. They called to one another, and jerked a thumb in her direction, but she paid no heed. She decided, though, to have the boy on the seat beside her. They were within two blocks of the Haymarket, on Randolph Street.
===Chinese===
他又睡着了。塞莉娜娴熟地驾着马穿过闹市区的街道。她睁着一双环顾四方的眼睛左顾右盼。别的货车从她旁边过去。前头已经排起了一列。男人们好奇地打量她。他们相互招呼，朝她那边扬扬大拇指，她全不理会。不过她打定主意，把男孩叫上来坐在自己身旁。离海马克特只隔两个街区了，已经在伦道夫街上。

===Original===
“Dirk! Come, now. Come up here with mother.” Grumbling, he climbed to the seat, yawned, smacked his lips, rubbed his knuckles into his eyes.
===Chinese===
「德克！来，来。上来到妈妈身边。」他嘟囔着爬上车座，打个哈欠，咂咂嘴，用指节揉眼睛。

===Original===
“What are we here for?”
===Chinese===
「咱们到这儿来干什么呀？」

===Original===
“So we can sell the garden truck and earn money.”
===Chinese===
「好把园里的菜卖掉，挣钱呀。」

===Original===
“What for?”
===Chinese===
「挣钱干什么？」

===Original===
“To send you to school to learn things.”
===Chinese===
「送你上学，学东西呀。」

===Original===
“That’s funny. I go to school already.”
===Chinese===
「那可怪了。我本来就上学呀。」

===Original===
“A different school. A big school.”
===Chinese===
「不一样的学校。一所大学校。」

===Original===
He was fully awake now, and looking about him interestedly. They turned into the Haymarket. It was a tangle of horses, carts, men. The wagons were streaming in from the German truck farms that lay to the north of Chicago as well as from the Dutch farms that lay to the southwest, whence Selina came. Fruits and vegetables﻿—tons of it﻿—acres of it﻿—piled in the wagons that blocked the historic square. An unarmed army bringing food to feed a great city. Through this little section, and South Water Street that lay to the east, passed all the verdant growing things that fed Chicago’s millions. Something of this came to Selina as she maneuvered her way through the throng. She felt a little thrill of significance, of achievement. She knew the spot she wanted for her own. Since that first trip to Chicago with Pervus in the early days of her marriage she had made the journey into town perhaps not more than a dozen times, but she had seen, and heard, and remembered. A place near the corner of Des Plaines, not at the curb, but rather in the double line of wagons that extended down the middle of the road. Here the purchasing peddlers and grocers had easy access to the wagons. Here Selina could display her wares to the best advantage. It was just across the way from Chris Spanknoebel’s restaurant, rooming house, and saloon. Chris knew her; had known Pervus for years and his father before him; would be kind to her and the boy in case of need.
===Chinese===
这会儿他全醒了，饶有兴味地四下张望。他们拐进了海马克特。那里是一团乱麻似的马、车和人。货车络绎不绝地涌进来，有从芝加哥以北的德国菜园来的，也有从西南面的荷兰农场来的——塞莉娜正是打那边来的。水果和蔬菜——成吨的——成亩的——堆在一辆辆堵住了那个载入史册的广场的货车上。一支不带兵器的军队，运着吃食来供养一座大城。芝加哥数百万人口吃的所有青翠生长的东西，都打这一小块地方，连同它东面的南水街（South Water Street），流进城去。塞莉娜在人群车马中间择路穿行时，多少体味到了这一层。她觉出一阵小小的、关乎身价的战栗，一阵成就的战栗。她知道自己想要哪个位置。自打新婚初期跟珀维斯头一回来芝加哥，她进城统共不过十来次，可她看过，听过，记住了。德斯普兰斯街（Des Plaines）拐角近旁一个位子，不在路缘，而在顺大路当中排下去的双列车队里。到这里，采买的货郎和杂货商们够得着各辆货车。在这里，塞莉娜能把她的货色抖搂到十二分的体面。对面正对着克里斯·斯潘克诺贝尔（Chris Spanknoebel）的饭铺、宿舍兼酒馆。克里斯认得她；认识珀维斯好些年，还认得珀维斯的父亲；万有用得着的时候，他会善待她和孩子。

===Original===
Dirk was wide awake now; eager, excited. The lights, the men, the horses, the sound of talk, and laughter, and clinking glasses from the eating houses along the street were bewilderingly strange to his country-bred eyes and ears. He called to the horses; stood up in the wagon; but clung closer to her as they found themselves in the thick of the melee.
===Chinese===
德克这下全醒了；热切，兴奋。灯火，男人，马匹，说话声，笑声，沿街饭馆里杯盏的叮当——对这双乡下生养的耳目，陌生得叫人发懵。他吆喝牲口；在车厢里站起来；可等他们陷进乱阵中心，他把她搂得更紧了。

===Original===
On the street corners where the lights were brightest there were stands at which men sold chocolate, cigars, collar buttons, suspenders, shoe strings, patent contrivances. It was like a fair. Farther down the men’s faces loomed mysteriously out of the half light. Stolid, sunburned faces now looked dark, terrifying, the whites of the eyes very white, the mustaches very black, their shoulders enormous. Here was a crap game beneath the street light. There stood two girls laughing and chatting with a policeman.
===Chinese===
在灯最亮的街角，摆着一处处摊子，男人们在那里卖巧克力、雪茄、领扣、背带、鞋带，还有各式新巧的专利玩意儿。简直像庙会。再往远处去，男人们的脸从半明半暗里神秘地浮凸出来。那些呆滞的、晒黑的脸此刻显得阴森可怖，眼白极白，髭须极黑，肩膀极宽。这边，街灯底下有一局掷骰子的赌。那边，两个姑娘站在那里，同一名警察又说又笑。

===Original===
“Here’s a good place, Mother. Here! There’s a dog on that wagon like Pom.”
===Chinese===
「这儿是个好位子，妈妈。这儿！那辆车上有一条狗，长得像波姆。」

===Original===
Pom, hearing his name, stood up, looked into the boy’s face, quivered, wagged a nervous tail, barked sharply. The Haymarket night life was an old story to Pom, but it never failed to stimulate him. Often he had guarded the wagon when Pervus was absent for a short time. He would stand on the seat ready to growl at anyone who so much as fingered a radish in Pervus’s absence.
===Chinese===
波姆听见了它的名字，站起来，端详男孩的脸，浑身一激灵，紧张地摆尾巴，尖声吠了两下。海马克特的夜生活对波姆早就是老故事了，可回回都照样提它的神。珀维斯短暂离开的时候，常常是它看车。它会站在车座上，只要有人胆敢在珀维斯不在时碰一碰一根萝卜，它就喉咙里滚出低吼。

===Original===
“Down, Pom! Quiet, Pom!” She did not want to attract attention to herself and the boy. It was still early. She had made excellent time. Pervus had often slept in snatches as he drove into town and the horses had lagged, but Selina had urged them on tonight. They had gained a good half hour over the usual time. Halfway down the block Selina espied the place she wanted. From the opposite direction came a truck farmer’s cart obviously making for the same stand. For the first time that night Selina drew the whip out of its socket and clipped sharply her surprised nags. With a start and a shuffle they broke into an awkward lope. Ten seconds too late the German farmer perceived her intention, whipped up his own tired team, arrived at the spot just as Selina, blocking the way, prepared to back into the vacant space.
===Chinese===
「趴下，波姆！安静，波姆！」她不想让她和孩子惹眼。天色还早。她这一路赶得出奇地顺。从前珀维斯赶车进城，常是一路打着盹，马也磨磨蹭蹭，可今夜塞莉娜一路催着。比平常足足抢出了半个钟头。走到这段街的半当腰，塞莉娜望见了她要的位子。对面方向正过来一辆菜农的车，明摆着也奔同一个摊位去。这一夜塞莉娜头一回把鞭子从插槽里抽出来，朝着她那两匹被抽得发愣的驽马清脆地就是一鞭。马儿一个趔趄、一阵倒腾，撒开一串踉跄的碎步小跑起来。晚了十秒，那德国菜农才看破她的意图，狠抽自己那对乏马，赶到地方时，塞莉娜已横在道上，正要倒车退进那块空位里去。

===Original===
“Heh, get out of there you﻿—” he roared; then, for the first time, perceived in the dim light of the street that his rival was a woman. He faltered, stared open-mouthed, tried other tactics. “You can’t go in there, missus.”
===Chinese===
「嘿，滚开，你——」他咆哮着；随即，在街灯昏光里头一回看清，对手竟是个女人。他噎了一下，张着嘴直瞪，另换了套说辞。「那个位子你进不去，太太。」

===Original===
“Oh, yes, I can.” She backed her team dexterously.
===Chinese===
「哦，我进得去。」她灵巧地把车倒了进去。

===Original===
“Yes, we can!” shouted Dirk in an attitude of fierce belligerence.
===Chinese===
「我们进得去！」德克呐喊，一副凶猛的临战架势。

===Original===
From the wagons on either side heads were lifted. “Where’s your man?” demanded the defeated driver, glaring.
===Chinese===
两边的货车上纷纷抬起头来。「你家男人呢？」落败的车夫瞪着眼质问。

===Original===
“Here,” replied Selina; put her hand on Dirk’s head.
===Chinese===
「在这儿，」塞莉娜答道；把手放在德克的头上。

===Original===
The other, preparing to drive on, received this with incredulity. He assumed the existence of a husband in the neighbourhood﻿—at Chris Spanknoebel’s probably, or talking prices with a friend at another wagon when he should be here attending to his own. In the absence of this, her natural protector, he relieved his disgruntled feelings as he gathered up the reins. “Woman ain’t got no business here in Haymarket, anyway. Better you’re home night time in your kitchen where you belong.”
===Chinese===
那一位本来就要赶车走了，闻言将信将疑。他方才只当她的男人就在左近什么地方——多半在克里斯·斯潘克诺贝尔那儿，要不就是趁该守着自家车的工夫，跑到别的车跟前跟朋友谈价钱。眼下这位天然保护人既然缺席，他便一边拢起缰绳，一边把一肚子不痛快放了出来。「反正娘们儿家就没有上海马克特来的份儿。夜里还是回你家厨房待着去，那才是你待的地方。」

===Original===
This admonition, so glibly mouthed by so many people in the past few days, now was uttered once too often. Selina’s nerves snapped. A surprised German truck farmer found himself being harangued from the driver’s seat of a vegetable wagon by an irate and fluent woman in a mashed black hat.
===Chinese===
这句劝诫，这几天被这么多人顺嘴滑舌地说来，如今偏偏又来说了一遍。塞莉娜的神经绷断了。一个错愕的德国菜农发现，自己竟被一个怒气冲冲、口若悬河、戴一顶压瘪黑帽的女人，站在一辆菜车的车座上劈头训斥。

===Original===
“Don’t talk to me like that, you great stupid! What good does it do a woman to stay home in her kitchen if she’s going to starve there, and her boy with her! Staying home in my kitchen won’t earn me any money. I’m here to sell the vegetables I helped raise and I’m going to do it. Get out of my way, you. Go along about your business or I’ll report you to Mike, the street policeman.”
===Chinese===
「不许这样跟我说话，你这个大蠢货！一个女人守在家里的厨房里挨饿，还有她儿子陪着一起挨饿，那守着还有什么好处！守在我家厨房里可挣不来一个子儿。我到这儿来，是卖我帮着种出来的菜，我这就卖。让开，你。办你的事去，不然我这就去告你，告到巡警迈克（Mike）那儿。」

===Original===
Now she clambered over the wagon wheel to unhitch the tired horses. It is impossible to tell what interpretation the dumbfounded north-sider put upon her movements. Certainly he had nothing to fear from this small gaunt creature with the blazing eyes. Nevertheless as he gathered up his reins terror was writ large on his rubicund face.
===Chinese===
这时她攀过车轮，去卸那两匹乏马。那位目瞪口呆的城北人如何解读她的这一连串动作，就无从知道了。当然，对这么个眼睛喷火、瘦小枯干的人，他实在没什么可怕的。然而他拢起缰绳时，那张红扑扑的脸上分明写着大写的恐惧。

===Original===
“*Teufel!* What a woman!” Was off in a clatter of wheels and hoofs on the cobblestones.
===Chinese===
「*Teufel!*（德语：魔鬼呀）好厉害的女人！」说话间，他已在鹅卵石路上辚辚的车轮和蹄声里去了。

===Original===
Selina unharnessed swiftly. “You stay here, Dirk, with Pom. Mother’ll be back in a minute.” She marched down the street driving the horses to the barns where, for twenty-five cents, the animals were to be housed in more comfort than their owner. She returned to find Dirk deep in conversation with two young women in red shirtwaists, plaid skirts that swept the ground, and sailor hats tipped at a saucy angle over pyramidal pompadours.
===Chinese===
塞莉娜飞快地卸了套。「你在这儿等着，德克，跟波姆一起。妈妈一会儿就回来。」她赶着两匹马沿街向牲口棚去——两毛五分钱，牲口住得比它们的主人还要舒坦。她回来时，见德克正同两位年轻女子聊得起劲：红色女衬衫（shirtwaist），长及扫地的格子裙，水手帽俏皮地斜扣在宝塔般高耸的盘发上。

===Original===
“I can’t make any sense out of it, can you, Elsie? Sounds like Dirt to me, but nobody’s going to name a kid that, are they? Stands to reason.”
===Chinese===
「我怎么听不明白呢，你呢，埃尔茜（Elsie）？我听着倒像『泥巴』（Dirt），可谁会给娃娃起这个名儿呢，是吧？道理明摆着嘛。」

===Original===
“Oh, come on. Your name’ll be mud first thing you know. Here it’s after nine already and not a﻿—” she turned and saw Selina’s white face.
===Chinese===
「得了，走吧。一转眼你就要落个臭名儿。都过九点了，还没——」她一转身，撞见了塞莉娜那张白脸。

===Original===
“There’s my mother,” said Dirk, triumphantly, pointing. The three women looked at each other. Two saw the pathetic hat and the dowdy clothes, and knew. One saw the red shirtwaists and the loose red lips, and knew.
===Chinese===
「那是我妈妈，」德克指着，得意洋洋。三个女人相互看了看。两个看见那顶寒碜的帽子和邋遢的衣裳，就都明白了。一个看见那两件红衬衫和两片放浪的红唇，也明白了。

===Original===
“We was just talking to the kid,” said the girl who had been puzzled by Dirk’s name. Her tone was defensive. “Just asking him his name, and like that.”
===Chinese===
「我们不过跟这小家伙说说话，」为德克的名字犯嘀咕的那个姑娘说，口气带着戒备。「就问问他的名字，什么的。」

===Original===
“His name is Dirk,” said Selina, mildly. “It’s a Dutch name﻿—Holland, you know. We’re from out High Prairie way, south. Dirk DeJong. I’m Mrs. DeJong.”
===Chinese===
「他叫德克，」塞莉娜温和地说。「是个荷兰名字——荷兰，知道吧。我们住高草原那边，在南边。德克·德容。我是德容太太。」

===Original===
“Yeh?” said the other girl. “I’m Elsie. Elsie from Chelsea, that’s me. Come on, Mabel. Stand gabbin’ all night.” She was blonde and shrill. The other was older, dark-haired. There was about her a paradoxical wholesomeness.
===Chinese===
「是嘛，」另一个姑娘说。「我是埃尔茜。切尔西（Chelsea）来的埃尔茜，说的就是我。走吧，玛贝尔（Mabel）。别站这儿穷聊一宿了。」她金头发，尖嗓门。另一个年长些，深色头发。她身上有一种自相矛盾的健全正派气。

===Original===
Mabel, the older one, looked at Selina sharply. From the next wagon came loud snores issuing from beneath the seat. From down the line where a lantern swung from the tailboard of a cart came the rattle of dice. “What you doing down here, anyway?”
===Chinese===
年长的那个，玛贝尔，锐利地打量塞莉娜。隔壁车的座位底下传出响亮的鼾声。队列那头，一辆车的尾板上悬着一盏晃动的灯笼，那边传来骰子的哗啦声。「你们到底下来这儿干什么？」

===Original===
“I’m here to sell my stuff tomorrow morning. Vegetables. From the farm.”
===Chinese===
「我明早要在这儿卖我的货。蔬菜。农庄上来的。」

===Original===
Mabel looked around. Hers was not a quick mind. “Where’s your man?”
===Chinese===
玛贝尔四下看了看。她脑子不快。「你家男人呢？」

===Original===
“My husband died a week ago.” Selina was making up their bed for the night. From beneath the seat she took a sack of hay, tight-packed, shook out its contents, spread them evenly on the floor of the wagon, at the front, first having unhinged the seat and clapped it against the wagon side as a headboard. Over the hay she spread empty sacking. She shook out her shawl, which would serve as cover. The girl Mabel beheld these preparations. Her dull eyes showed a gleam of interest which deepened to horror.
===Chinese===
「我丈夫一个星期前去世了。」塞莉娜正在给他们铺今晚的床。她从座位底下拖出一袋压得瓷实的干草，抖散了，均匀铺在车厢前头的地板上——先把车座的铰链卸开，把座板竖起来靠在车厢侧板上权当床头。草上再铺一层空麻袋。她抖开披肩，权当被子。玛贝尔姑娘眼看她这一番铺排。她那双发迟的眼睛先亮起一点兴味，随即加深成骇然。

===Original===
“Say, you ain’t never going to sleep out here, are you? You and the kid. Like that!”
===Chinese===
「喂，你们总不会真要睡在外头这儿吧，啊？你跟这孩子。就这么着！」

===Original===
“Yes.”
===Chinese===
「是的。」

===Original===
“Well, for﻿—” She stared, turned to go, came back. From her belt that dipped so stylishly in the front hung an arsenal of jangling metal articles﻿—purse, pencil, mirror, comb﻿—a chatelaine, they called it. She opened the purse now and took from it a silver dollar. This she tendered Selina, almost roughly. “Here. Get the kid a decent roost for the night. You and the kid, see.”
===Chinese===
「哎呀，这——」她瞪着眼，转身要走，又回来了。她那条在前腰时髦地凹下去的腰带上，挂着一串叮当乱响的金属家什——钱包、铅笔、镜子、梳子——他们管这个叫腰坠挂链（chatelaine）。这时她打开钱包，摸出一块银元。她把它塞给塞莉娜，几乎可以说是粗暴地。「拿着。给孩子找个像样的窝过这一夜。你跟孩子，听见没。」

===Original===
Selina stared at the shining round dollar; at Mabel’s face. The quick sting of tears came to her eyes. She shook her head, smiled. “We don’t mind sleeping out here. Thank you just the same﻿—Mabel.”
===Chinese===
塞莉娜盯着那枚亮闪闪的圆银元；盯着玛贝尔的脸。泪水迅疾地刺上她的眼睛。她摇摇头，笑了。「睡在外头这儿我们不介意。还是谢谢你——玛贝尔。」

===Original===
The girl put her dollar plumply back into her purse. “Well, takes all kinds, I always say. I thought I had a bum deal but, say, alongside of what you got I ain’t got it so worse. Place to sleep in, anyways, even if it is﻿—well, good night. Listen to that Elsie, hollering for me. I’m comin’! Shut up!”
===Chinese===
姑娘把那块银元一屁股塞回钱包。「唉，人各有各的活法，我总这么说。我还当我这命够苦的，可我说，跟你这份儿比，我这也不算顶坏。好歹有个睡觉的地方，哪怕它是——唉，晚安吧。听埃尔茜那嗓门，喊我呢。来了！闭嘴！」

===Original===
You heard the two on their way up the street, arm in arm, laughing.
===Chinese===
你听得见那两个人沿街远去，挽着胳膊，一路笑着。

===Original===
“Come Dirk.”
===Chinese===
「来，德克。」

===Original===
“Are we going to sleep here!” He was delighted.
===Chinese===
「我们要睡在这儿啦！」他乐坏了。

===Original===
“Right here, all snug in the hay, like campers.”
===Chinese===
「就在这儿，干草里舒舒服服，像野营的人。」

===Original===
The boy lay down, wriggling, laughing. “Like gypsies. Ain’t it, Mom?”
===Chinese===
男孩躺下，扭着，笑着。「像吉普赛人。是不是，妈？」

===Original===
“ ‘Isn’t it,’ Dirk﻿—not ‘ain’t it.’ ” The school teacher.
===Chinese===
「要说 isn’t it，德克——不是 ain’t it。」女教师的口气又冒出来了。

===Original===
She lay down beside him. The boy seemed terribly wide awake. “I liked the Mabel one best, didn’t you? She was the nicest, h’m?”
===Chinese===
她在他身边躺下。男孩看来精神得不得了。「我最喜欢玛贝尔那个，你呢？她顶好，嗯？」

===Original===
“Oh, much the nicest,” said Selina, and put one arm around him and drew him to her, close. And suddenly he was asleep, deeply. The street became quieter. The talking and laughter ceased. The lights were dim at Chris Spanknoebel’s. Now and then the clatter of wheels and horses’ hoofs proclaimed a late comer seeking a place, but the sound was not near by, for this block and those to east and west were filled by now. These men had been up at four that morning, must be up before four the next.
===Chinese===
「哦，顶好顶好的，」塞莉娜说，伸出一条胳膊搂住他，把他拉近，紧紧的。忽然间他就睡着了，睡得沉沉的。街上静下来了。说话声笑声歇了。克里斯·斯潘克诺贝尔那边的灯火已经暗了。间或一阵车轮马蹄的响动，宣告一个迟来的还在找位子，但声音离得不近，因为这一段街和东西两段这会儿都停满了。这些人今天凌晨四点就起的身，明儿还得四点以前爬起来。

===Original===
The night was cool, but not cold. Overhead you saw the wide strip of sky between the brick buildings on either side of the street. Two men came along singing. “Shut up!” growled a voice from a wagon along the curb. The singers subsided. It must be ten o’clock and after, Selina thought. She had with her Pervus’s nickel watch, but it was too dark to see its face, and she did not want to risk a match. Measured footsteps that passed and repassed at regular intervals. The night policeman.
===Chinese===
夜里凉，但不冷。抬头看得见街道两侧砖楼之间那一长条天空。两个男人一路唱着歌过来。「闭嘴！」路边一辆货车里一个声音咕哝。唱歌的没了声。该是十点过了，塞莉娜想。她身边带着珀维斯那只镍壳怀表，可是天太黑看不见表盘，她又不想白白划火柴冒险。有节奏的脚步声，隔一阵便走过、又走回。夜班警察。

===Original===
She lay looking up at the sky. There were no tears in her eyes. She was past tears. She thought, “Here I am, Selina Peake, sleeping in a wagon, in the straw, like a bitch with my puppy snuggled beside me. I was going to be like Jo in Louisa Alcott’s book. On my feet are boots and on my body a dyed dress. How terribly long it is going to be until morning﻿ ﻿… I must try to sleep.﻿ ﻿… I must try to sleep﻿ ﻿…”
===Chinese===
她躺着，仰望天空。她眼里没有泪。她已经过了流泪的阶段。她想，「我塞莉娜·皮克，如今睡在一辆货车里，睡在干草上，像条母狗，狗崽子偎在身边。我本来要活成路易莎·奥尔科特（Louisa Alcott）书里的乔（Jo）那样。如今我脚上是长靴，身上是染过的衣裳。离天亮还不知有多久，多久啊……我得想法睡。……我得想法睡。……」

===Original===
She did sleep, miraculously. The September stars twinkled brightly down on them. As she lay there, the child in her arms, asleep, peace came to the haggard face, relaxed the tired limbs. Much like another woman who had lain in the straw with her child in her arms almost two thousand years before.

===Chinese===
她居然睡着了，简直是奇迹。九月的星星明亮地朝他们眨眼。她躺在那里，孩子在臂弯里睡着了，安宁来到那张憔悴的脸上，松弛了疲惫的四肢。很像另一个女人，差不多两千年前，也曾怀里抱着她的孩子，躺在干草上。

## XI / 第十一章

===Original===
It would be enchanting to be able to record that Selina, next day, had phenomenal success, disposing of her carefully bunched wares to great advantage, driving smartly off up Halsted Street toward High Prairie with a goodly profit jingling in her scuffed leather purse. The truth is that she had a day so devastating, so catastrophic, as would have discouraged most men and certainly any woman less desperate and determined.
===Chinese===
要是能这样写下一笔该多叫人欢喜：第二天，塞莉娜吉星高照，大获其利，把她精心捆扎的菜蔬卖了个好价钱，赶着车利利索索，沿哈尔斯特德街直奔高草原，磨旧的皮钱包里叮当作响，装着一笔可观的进项。可实情是，她碰上的这一天摧折人到了这般地步，简直是一场大难，多数男人也要被它磨得泄气，任何绝望与狠劲不及她的女人，则断然撑持不下来。

===Original===
She had awakened, not to daylight, but to the three o’clock blackness. The street was already astir. Selina brushed her skirt to rid it of the clinging hay, tidied herself as best she could. Leaving Dirk still asleep, she called Pom from beneath the wagon to act as sentinel at the dashboard, and crossed the street to Chris Spanknoebel’s. She knew Chris, and he her. He would let her wash at the faucet at the rear of the eating house. She would buy hot coffee for herself and Dirk to warm and revivify them. They would eat the sandwiches left from the night before.
===Chinese===
她醒来时，不见天光，只有凌晨三点的漆黑。街上已经活动起来。塞莉娜刷去裙上粘着的干草，尽力把自己拾掇齐整。德克还睡着，她把波姆从车底下唤出来，叫它在车前的挡板上当哨兵，自己过街去克里斯·斯潘克诺贝尔的铺子。她认得克里斯，克里斯也认得她。他会让她在饭馆后头的水龙头下洗漱。她要给自己和德克买热咖啡，暖暖身子、醒醒神。头天夜里剩下的三明治正好当早点。

===Original===
Chris himself, a pot-paunched Austrian, blond, benevolent, was standing behind his bar, wiping the slab with a large moist cloth. With the other hand he swept the surface with a rubber-tipped board about the size of a shingle. This contrivance gathered up such beads of moisture as might be left by the cloth. Two sweeps of it rendered the counter dry and shining. Later Chris allowed Dirk to wield this rubber-tipped contrivance﻿—a most satisfactory thing to do, leaving one with a feeling of perfect achievement.
===Chinese===
克里斯本人是个大腹便便的奥地利人，金头发，一脸和气，正站在柜台后头，用一大块湿布擦拭台面。另一只手里，他拿一块胶皮头的小板——约莫一片墙面板大小——把台面刮一遍。这件家伙什儿，专收布擦过后剩下的水珠子。刮上两下，柜台便又干又亮。后来克里斯把这块胶皮刮板交给德克耍——这可是件再叫人满意不过的差事，干完了，心里只觉得成就圆满。

===Original===
Spanknoebel seemed never to sleep, yet his colour was ruddy, his blue eyes clear. The last truckster coming in at night for a beer or a cup of coffee and a sandwich was greeted by Chris, white-aproned, pink-cheeked, wide awake, swabbing the bar’s shining surface with the thirsty cloth, swishing it with the sly rubber-tipped board. “Well, how goes it all the while?” said Chris. The earliest morning trader found Chris in a fresh white apron crackling with starch and ironing. He would swab the bar with a gesture of welcome, of greeting. “Well, how goes it all the while?”
===Chinese===
斯潘克诺贝尔仿佛从来不睡觉，气色却红润，蓝眼睛清亮。夜里最后一个进店来喝杯啤酒、或者要杯咖啡加一份三明治的赶车菜农，会受到克里斯的迎接——他系着白围裙，红脸膛，毫无睡意，正用那块吸水的布揩拭柜台发亮的台面，又用那块乖巧的胶皮刮板嗖嗖刮过。「哎，这一向可好啊？」克里斯说。清早头一个到的贩子，看见的克里斯已经换上一条新白围裙，浆得挺括，熨得平整。他会带着迎客、打招呼的手势擦一把柜台。「哎，这一向可好啊？」

===Original===
As Selina entered the long room now there was something heartening, reassuring about Chris’s clean white apron, his ruddy colour, the very sweep of his shirt-sleeved arm as it encompassed the bar-slab. From the kitchen at the rear came the sounds of sizzling and frying, and the gracious scent of coffee and of frying pork and potatoes. Already the market men were seated at the tables eating huge and hurried breakfasts: hunks of ham; eggs in pairs; potatoes cut in great cubes; cups of steaming coffee and chunks of bread that they plastered liberally with butter.
===Chinese===
此刻塞莉娜走进这间狭长的店堂，克里斯那条干净的白围裙，他那红润的气色，乃至他那只挽着衬衫袖、一挥便扫过整块柜台的手臂，都自有一种叫人提气、叫人安心的味道。后厨传来滋滋的煎炸声，咖啡和煎猪肉煎土豆的香气好闻极了。集市上的汉子们已经落座开饭，早饭量大而匆忙：大块的火腿；成双的鸡蛋；切作大方块的土豆；一杯杯冒着热气的咖啡，还有一块块面包，黄油抹得足足的。

===Original===
Selina approached Chris. His round face loomed out through the smoke like the sun in a fog. “Well, how goes it all the while?” Then he recognized her. “*Um Gottes!*﻿—why, it’s Mis’ DeJong!” He wiped his great hand on a convenient towel, extended it in sympathy to the widow. “I heerd,” he said, “I heerd.” His inarticulateness made his words doubly effective.
===Chinese===
塞莉娜朝克里斯走过去。他的圆脸从烟雾里浮现出来，像雾中的太阳。「哎，这一向可好啊？」接着他认出了她。「*Um Gottes!*（德语：老天爷呀）——哎呀，这不是德容太太嘛！」他在手边一条毛巾上擦了擦那只大手，满含同情地朝寡妇伸过来。「俺听说了，」他说，「俺听说了。」他这份讷于言辞，反倒叫他的话加倍有分量。

===Original===
“I’ve come in with the load, Mr. Spanknoebel. The boy and I. He’s still asleep in the wagon. May I bring him over here to clean him up a little before breakfast?”
===Chinese===
「我拉车菜进城来了，斯潘克诺贝尔先生。我带着孩子呢。他还在车上睡着。我能带他过来，趁早饭前拾掇拾掇吗？」

===Original===
“Sure! Sure!” A sudden suspicion struck him. “You ain’t slept in the wagon, Mis’ DeJong! *Um Gottes!*﻿—”
===Chinese===
「那还用说！那还用说！」他忽然起了个疑心。「你们不是就在车上睡的吧，德容太太！*Um Gottes!*——」

===Original===
“Yes. It wasn’t bad. The boy slept the night through. I slept, too, quite a little.”
===Chinese===
「就是。不算坏。孩子一觉睡到大天亮。我也睡了，还睡了不小一阵呢。」

===Original===
“Why you didn’t come here! Why﻿—” At the look in Selina’s face he knew then. “For nothing you and the boy could sleep here.”
===Chinese===
「那你干吗不来这儿！干吗——」一见塞莉娜脸上的神色，他登时明白了。「你跟孩子本来可以白住在这儿的。」

===Original===
“I knew that! That’s why.”
===Chinese===
「我正是知道这个！所以才不来。」

===Original===
“Don’t talk dumb, Mrs. DeJong. Half the time the rooms is vacant. You and the boy chust as well﻿—twenty cents, then, and pay me when you got it. But anyway you don’t come in reg’lar with the load, do you? That ain’t for womans.”
===Chinese===
「别说傻话了，德容太太。那些屋子多一半儿时候都空着。你跟孩子只管来住——那就两毛钱，什么时候有了什么时候给。不过说回来，你总不能回回都拉车菜来，啊？那可不是娘们儿家干的营生。」

===Original===
“There’s no one to do it for me, except Jan. And he’s worse than nobody. Just through September and October. After that, maybe﻿—” Her voice trailed off. It is hard to be hopeful at three in the morning, before breakfast.
===Chinese===
「没人能替我干这个，除了扬。可他还不如没有人。也就九月、十月里赶一赶。往后，兴许——」她的声音低了下去。凌晨三点，早饭之前，人是不容易往好处想的。

===Original===
She went to the little wash room at the rear, felt better immediately she had washed vigorously, combed her hair. She returned to the wagon to find a panic-stricken Dirk sure of nothing but that he had been deserted by his mother. Fifteen minutes later the two were seated at a table on which was spread what Chris Spanknoebel considered an adequate breakfast. A heartening enough beginning for the day, and a deceptive.
===Chinese===
她到后头那间小盥洗室去，痛痛快快洗了一把，又梳了头发，登时觉得好受些了。她回到车边，只见德克吓慌了神，一门心思只道是妈妈把他丢下了。一刻钟后，娘儿俩已在一张桌前坐定，桌上铺陈开克里斯·斯潘克诺贝尔心目中一顿像样的早餐。这一天有个这样提气的开头倒也够了——可惜这开头是个幌子。

===Original===
The Haymarket buyers did not want to purchase its vegetables from Selina DeJong. It wasn’t used to buying of women, but to selling to them. Peddlers and small grocers swarmed in at four﻿—Greeks, Italians, Jews. They bought shrewdly, craftily, often dishonestly. They sold their wares to the housewives. Their tricks were many. They would change a box of tomatoes while your back was turned; filch a head of cauliflower. There was little system or organization.
===Chinese===
海马克特的买主们不想从塞莉娜·德容手里买菜。这市场不惯于向女人买进，只惯于向女人卖出。四点钟光景，小贩和小杂货店主们蜂拥而来——希腊人、意大利人、犹太人。他们买得精明、刁钻，常常不讲信义。他们把货再卖给各家主妇。他们的花招多得很。趁你一转身，他们能调换一箱番茄；顺走一棵菜花。这里头没有什么规矩章法。

===Original===
Take Luigi. Luigi peddled on the north side. He called his wares through the alleys and side streets of Chicago, adding his raucous voice to the din of an inchoate city. A swarthy face had Luigi, a swift brilliant smile, a crafty eye. The Haymarket called him Loogy. When prices did not please Luigi he pretended not to understand. Then the Haymarket would yell, undeceived, “Heh, Loogy, what de mattah! Spika da Engleesh!” They knew him.
===Chinese===
就拿路易吉（Luigi）来说。路易吉在城北串巷子卖菜。他沿着芝加哥的小巷和偏街吆喝他的货，把一副沙嗓子添进这座尚在草创的城市的一片喧闹里。路易吉生着一张黑黝黝的脸，一个倏忽而灿烂的笑，一双刁钻的眼睛。海马克特的人都管他叫「路吉」（Loogy）。价钱不称路易吉的心，他就装作听不懂。海马克特众人便不上当，冲他嚷：「嘿，路吉，咋的啦！说英格利希呀！」他的底细他们是知道的。

===Original===
Selina had taken the covers off her vegetables. They were revealed crisp, fresh, colourful. But Selina knew they must be sold now, quickly. When the leaves began to wilt, when the edges of the cauliflower heads curled ever so slightly, turned brown and limp, their value decreased by half, even though the heads themselves remained white and firm.
===Chinese===
塞莉娜已把菜上的苫布掀开。菜蔬露了相：脆嫩、新鲜、水灵鲜亮。可塞莉娜知道，必须趁现在赶紧脱手。菜叶一打蔫，菜花球儿的边缘哪怕稍稍卷起、转褐、耷拉下来，价钱就得对半折——哪怕菜球本身还又白又硬。

===Original===
Down the street came the buyers﻿—little black-eyed swarthy men; plump, shirt-sleeved, greasy men; shrewd, tobacco-chewing men in overalls. Stolid red Dutch faces, sunburned. Lean dark foreign faces. Shouting, clatter, turmoil.
===Chinese===
买主们沿街涌过来——黑眼睛黑皮肤的小个子男人；胖乎乎、挽着袖子、油光光的男人；工装裤里嚼着烟草的精明男人。红脸膛的荷兰人脸，晒得黑红，愣头愣脑。精瘦黝黑的外国脸。吆喝，嘈杂，乱作一团。

===Original===
“Heh! Get your horse outta here! What the hell!”
===Chinese===
「嘿！把你那匹马弄出去！搞什么名堂！」

===Original===
“How much for the whole barrel?”
===Chinese===
「整桶什么价？」

===Original===
“Got any beans? No, don’t want no cauliflower. Beans!”
===Chinese===
「有豆子没？不，菜花不稀罕。豆子！」

===Original===
“Tough!”
===Chinese===
「活该！」

===Original===
“Well, keep ’em. I don’t want ’em.”
===Chinese===
「那好，留着吧。我不要。」

===Original===
“Quarter for the sack.”
===Chinese===
「一袋两毛五。」

===Original===
“G’wan, them ain’t five-pound heads. Bet they don’t come four pounds to the head.”
===Chinese===
「去你的，这哪是五磅一颗的。我赌一颗准不到四磅。」

===Original===
“Who says they don’t!”
===Chinese===
「谁说不到！」

===Original===
“Gimme five bushels them.”
===Chinese===
「那个给我来五蒲式耳。」

===Original===
Food for Chicago’s millions. In and out of the wagons. Under horses’ hoofs. Barefooted children, baskets on their arms, snatching bits of fallen vegetables from the cobbles. Gutter Annie, a shawl pinned across her pendulous breasts, scavengering a potato there, an onion fallen to the street, scraps of fruit and green stuff in the ditch. Big Kate buying carrots, parsley, turnips, beets, all slightly wilted and cheap, which she would tie into bunches with her bit of string and sell to the real grocers for soup greens.
===Chinese===
这是喂芝加哥几百万人的口粮。在货车间穿进穿出。在马蹄底下。赤脚的孩子们挎着篮子，从鹅卵石缝里抢拾掉落的碎菜。阴沟安妮（Gutter Annie），披肩用别针在松垂的胸前别住，这儿拾一个土豆，那儿捡一个滚到街上的洋葱头，再把阴沟里零星的水果和青菜残叶扒拉走。大凯特（Big Kate）正买胡萝卜、欧芹、芜菁、甜菜，全是些微微发蔫的便宜货，回头用她那一小段绳子扎成一束一束，卖给正牌杂货铺，充作做汤的青菜。

===Original===
The day broke warm. The sun rose red. It would be a humid September day such as frequently came in the autumn to this lake region. Garden stuff would have to move quickly this morning. Afternoon would find it worthless.
===Chinese===
天亮了，暖烘烘的。太阳红彤彤地升起来。这将是个湿闷的九月天，这样的日子，秋天里常光临这湖区。园子里的菜今儿上午非赶紧出手不可。到了下午就一钱不值了。

===Original===
Selina stationed herself by her wagon. She saw the familiar faces of a half dozen or more High Prairie neighbours. These called to her, or came over briefly to her wagon, eyeing her wares with a calculating glance. “How you making out, Mis’ DeJong? Well, you got a good load there. Move it along quick this morning. It’s going to be hot I betcha.” Their tone was kindly, but disapproving, too. Their look said, “No place for a woman. No place for a woman.”
===Chinese===
塞莉娜在自家车旁站定。她看见了六七张高草原乡邻的熟脸。他们远远招呼她，或者短促地到她车前站一站，拿掂估的眼光把她的货一打量。「卖得怎么样啊，德容太太？唔，你这车菜不赖。今儿早上快些脱手吧。我敢保这天儿要热。」他们口气是和气的，可也带着不以为然。他们的眼神在说：「不是女人待的地方。不是女人待的地方。」

===Original===
The peddlers looked at her bunched bouquets, glanced at her, passed her by. It was not unkindness that prompted them, but a certain shyness, a fear of the unaccustomed. They saw her pale fine face with its great sombre eyes; the slight figure in the decent black dress; the slim brown hands clasped so anxiously together. Her wares were tempting but they passed her by with the instinct that the ignorant have against that which is unusual.
===Chinese===
小贩们望望她那一把把扎得像花束似的菜，瞟她一眼，走了过去。促着他们的并非狠心，倒是一种羞怯，一种对不惯之事的畏怯。他们看见她那张苍白清秀的脸和那双幽沉的大眼睛；那瘦小的身影裹在一身素净的黑裙里；那两只棕色细手焦灼地攥在一处。她的货馋人，可他们仍旧擦身而过，怀着无知者对异乎寻常之物的本能避忌。

===Original===
By nine o’clock trading began to fall off. In a panic Selina realized that the sales she had made amounted to little more than two dollars. If she stayed there until noon she might double that, but no more. In desperation she harnessed the horses, threaded her way out of the swarming street, and made for South Water Street farther east. Here were the commission houses. The district was jammed with laden carts and wagons exactly as the Haymarket had been, but trading was done on a different scale. She knew that Pervus had sometimes left his entire load with an established dealer here, to be sold on commission. She remembered the name﻿—Talcott﻿—though she did not know the exact location.
===Chinese===
到九点钟，买卖开始冷下来。塞莉娜恐慌地发觉，自己卖到手的统共不过两块钱挂零。若在这儿捱到晌午，兴许能翻上一番，可也就到头了。她横下心套上马，从熙熙攘攘的街里挤出去，奔更东边的南水街。代卖行都在那儿。这一带同样塞满了满载的板车和货车，跟海马克特一个模样，只是买卖另是一番路数。她知道珀维斯从前有时把整车货撂给这儿一家立住字号的行家，托他代卖。那名字她记得——塔尔科特——只是不知道铺子究竟在哪块儿。

===Original===
“Where we going now, Mom?” The boy had been almost incredibly patient and good. He had accepted his bewildering new surroundings with the adaptability of childhood. He had revelled richly in Chris Spanknoebel’s generous breakfast. He had thought the four dusty artificial palms that graced Chris’s back room luxuriantly tropical. He had been fascinated by the kitchen with its long glowing range, its great tables for slicing, paring, cutting. He liked the ruddy cheer of it, the bustle, the mouth-watering smells. At the wagon he had stood sturdily next his mother, had busied himself vastly assisting her in her few pitiful sales; had plucked wilted leaves, brought forward the freshest and crispest vegetables. But now she saw that he was drooping a little as were her wares, with the heat and the absence from accustomed soil. “Where we going now, Mom?”
===Chinese===
「这会儿咱们上哪儿去，妈？」这孩子一直耐心得、乖顺得简直叫人难以相信。他以孩童的随遇而安，领受了那叫他发懵的全新环境。克里斯·斯潘克诺贝尔那顿丰盛早饭，他大大地受用了一番。他觉得克里斯后屋那四盆落满灰尘、聊充摆设的假棕榈，好一派葱茏的热带风光。那间厨房也叫他看住了：长长的炉灶烧得通红，一张张大案子，切片、削皮、切菜全用它们。他喜欢那里头红火的欢快劲儿，那份忙乱，那些勾人馋虫的香味。在车旁，他结结实实地挨着妈妈站，煞有介事地大帮其忙，帮她做成那几笔可怜巴巴的买卖；掐掉蔫叶子，把最新鲜最脆生的菜往前头挪。可这会儿她看见他也有点儿蔫了，跟她的菜一个样，是热出来的，也是离了熟土的缘故。「这会儿咱们上哪儿去，妈？」

===Original===
“To another street, Sobig﻿—”
===Chinese===
「去另一条街，这么大——」

===Original===
“Dirk!”
===Chinese===
「德克！」

===Original===
“﻿—Dirk, where there’s a man who’ll buy all our stuff at once﻿—maybe. Won’t that be fine! Then we’ll go home. You help mother find his name over the store. Talcott﻿—T-a-l-c-o-double t.”
===Chinese===
「——德克，那条街上有个老爷，兴许能一口气把咱们的菜全买下——也许吧。那多好！然后咱们就回家。你帮妈妈找铺子招牌上他的名字。塔尔科特——T-a-l-c-o，双写 t。」

===Original===
South Water Street was changing with the city’s growth. Yankee names they used to be﻿—Flint﻿—Keen﻿—Rusk﻿—Lane. Now you saw Cuneo﻿—Meleges﻿—Garibaldi﻿—Campagna. There it was: William Talcott. Fruits and Vegetables.
===Chinese===
南水街正随着城市的扩张改换着面目。这里从前净是扬基姓氏——弗林特——基恩——拉斯克——莱恩。如今你瞧见的，是库内奥——梅莱杰斯——加里波第——坎帕尼亚。喏，就在那儿：威廉·塔尔科特（William Talcott），果品与蔬菜。

===Original===
William Talcott, standing in the cool doorway of his great deep shed-like store, was the antithesis of the feverish crowded street which he so calmly surveyed. He had dealt for forty years in provender. His was the unruffled demeanour of a man who knows the world must have what he has to sell. Every weekday morning at six his dim shaded cavern of a store was packed with sacks, crates, boxes, barrels from which peeped ruffles and sprigs of green; flashes of scarlet, plum-colour, orange. He bought the best only; sold at high prices. He had known Pervus, and Pervus’s father before him, and had adjudged them honest, admirable men. But of their garden truck he had small opinion. The Great Lakes boats brought him choice Michigan peaches and grapes; refrigerator cars brought him the products of California’s soil in a day when out-of-season food was a rare luxury. He wore neat pepper-and-salt pants and vest; shirt sleeves a startling white in that blue-shirted overalled world; a massive gold watch chain spanning his middle; square-toed boots; a straw fedora set well back; a pretty good cigar, unlighted, in his mouth. Shrewd blue eyes he had; sparse hair much the colour of his suit. Like a lean laconic god he stood in his doorway niche while toilers offered for his inspection the fruits of the earth.
===Chinese===
威廉·塔尔科特站在他那又深又大、棚库似的铺子阴凉的门洞里，跟他正冷眼打量的这条狂热拥挤的街恰成对照。他做粮菜营生四十年了。他那一副纹丝不乱的气度，属于深知世人非得买他手里的货不可的人。每个工作日早晨六点，他那幽暗遮荫、洞窟一般的铺子里就塞满了麻袋、条板箱、箱子和桶，缝隙里探出一褶一褶、一撮一撮的绿；闪出猩红、紫红、橙黄。他只进头等货；卖头等价。他认得珀维斯，先前还认得珀维斯的父亲，断定他们都是诚实可敬的人。可对他们园子里的菜，他向来不甚看重。五大湖的轮船给他运来上好的密歇根桃子和葡萄；冷藏车给他运来加利福尼亚的土产——在那个反季吃食还算稀罕奢侈品的年代。他穿一身齐整的胡椒盐色裤子和马甲；衬衫袖子在那个蓝衬衫、工装裤的世界里白得扎眼；一条粗大的金表链横担在腰间；方头的靴子；一顶草编软呢帽朝后戴着；嘴里叼一支相当好的雪茄，没点着。他生着一双精明的蓝眼睛；稀稀拉拉的头发跟那一身衣裳一个颜色。他站在门洞这方壁龛里，像一尊清瘦寡言的神祇，任劳碌人把大地的出产呈上来，听凭他验看。

===Original===
“Nope. Can’t use that lot, Jake. Runty. H’m. Wa-a-al, guess you’d better take them farther up the street, Tunis. Edges look kind of brown. Wilty.”
===Chinese===
「不中。这批货使不得，杰克。长不成个儿。唔。呃——得了，特尼斯，你最好把它们拉到街那头去。边儿上瞧着有点发褐。蔫了。」

===Original===
Stewards from the best Chicago hotels of that day﻿—the Sherman House, the Auditorium, the Palmer House, the Wellington, the Stratford﻿—came to Will Talcott for their daily supplies. The grocers who catered to the well-to-do north-side families and those in the neighbourhood of fashionable Prairie Avenue on the south bought of him.
===Chinese===
当年芝加哥一流旅馆的采买——谢尔曼旅馆（Sherman House）、大会堂旅馆、帕尔默旅馆、惠灵顿（Wellington）、斯特拉福德（Stratford）——日常的伙食都到威尔·塔尔科特这儿来办。那些专伺候城北殷实人家的杂货铺，还有南边时髦的草原大道左近的铺子，都从他手里进货。

===Original===
Now, in his doorway, he eyed the spare little figure that appeared before him all in rusty black, with its strained anxious face, its great deep-sunk eyes.
===Chinese===
这会儿，他在门洞里，把出现在他眼前的那瘦小身影打量了一番：一身穿旧发乌的黑衣，一张紧绷焦灼的脸，一双深深下陷的大眼睛。

===Original===
“DeJong, eh? Sorry to hear about your loss, ma’am. Pervus was a fine lad. No great shakes at truck farming, though. His widow, h’m? Hm.” Here, he saw, was no dull-witted farm woman; no stolid Dutch woman truckster. He went out to her wagon, tweaked the boy’s brown cheek. “Wa-al now, Mis’ DeJong, you got a right smart lot of garden stuff here and it looks pretty good. Yessir, pretty good. But you’re too late. Ten, pret’ near.”
===Chinese===
「德容家的，嗯？您遭了大故，很替您难过，太太。珀维斯是个好后生。不过种菜嘛，算不得大本事。他留下的寡妇，唔？嗯。」他看得出，眼前这一位不是个愚钝的农妇；不是个麻木的荷兰种菜婆子。他走到她车边，拧了拧孩子的棕色脸颊。「哎——我说，德容太太，你这儿好大一批园子菜，瞧着还挺不赖。可不，挺不赖。可你来晚啦。十点啦，差不离。」

===Original===
“Oh, no!” cried Selina. “Oh, no! Not too late!” And at the agony in her voice he looked at her sharply.
===Chinese===
「哦，不！」塞莉娜喊道。「哦，不！不算晚！」听出她话音里的痛楚，他锐利地看了她一眼。

===Original===
“Tell you what, mebbe I can move half of ’em along for you. But stuff don’t keep this weather. Turns wilty and my trade won’t touch it﻿ ﻿… First trip in?”
===Chinese===
「这么着吧，兴许我能替你打发掉一半。可这天气菜搁不住。一发蔫，我的主顾就不沾了……头一回来？」

===Original===
She wiped her face that was damp and yet cold to the touch. “First﻿—trip in.” Suddenly she was finding it absurdly hard to breathe.
===Chinese===
她抹了一把脸——那脸摸上去又湿又凉。「头一——回来。」忽然间她只觉得喘气费劲，费劲得没了道理。

===Original===
He called from the sidewalk to the men within: “George! Ben! Hustle this stuff in. Half of it. The best. Send you check tomorrow, Mis’ DeJong. Picked a bad day, didn’t you, for your first day?”
===Chinese===
他隔着人行道朝里头的人喊：「乔治！本！把这些搬进来。一半。要最好的。明天给你送支票去，德容太太。头一天就赶上这么个日子，是不是？」

===Original===
“Hot, you mean?”
===Chinese===
「您是说天热？」

===Original===
“Wa-al, hot, yes. But I mean a holiday like this peddlers mostly ain’t buying.”
===Chinese===
「呃——热，是热。可我是说赶上这么个节日，小贩们多半不进货。」

===Original===
“Holiday?”
===Chinese===
「节日？」

===Original===
“You knew it was a Jew holiday, didn’t you? Didn’t!﻿—Wa-al, my sakes! Worst day in the year. Jew peddlers all at church today and all the others not peddlers bought in Saturday for two days. Chicken men down the street got empty coops and will have till tomorrow. Yessir. Biggest chicken eaters, Jews are, in the world﻿ ﻿… Hm﻿ ﻿… Better just drive along home and just dump the rest that stuff, my good woman.”
===Chinese===
「你知道今儿是犹太人的节日吧？不知道！——哎呀我的天！一年里顶顶糟的日子。犹太小贩今儿全做礼拜去了，其余不贩菜的，星期六就把两天的货都办齐了。街那头卖鸡的，笼子空着，还得空到明儿。可不。世界上吃鸡吃得最凶的，就数犹太人……唔……干脆赶车家去，剩下的菜就倒了吧，我的好太太。」

===Original===
One hand on the seat she prepared to climb up again﻿—did step to the hub. You saw her shabby, absurd side-boots that were so much too big for the slim little feet. “If you’re just buying my stuff because you’re sorry for me﻿—” The Peake pride.
===Chinese===
她一只手搭在车座上，预备再爬上去——一只脚已经踩上轮毂了。这时你才看清她那双寒碜可笑的偏扣靴，对她那双纤小的脚来说，大得实在离谱。「您要是单只因为可怜我才买我的菜——」皮克家的傲气。

===Original===
“Don’t do business that way. Can’t afford to, ma’am. My da’ter she’s studying to be a singer. In Italy now, Car’line is, and costs like all get-out. Takes all the money I can scrape together, just about.”
===Chinese===
「我做买卖不兴那一套。也赔不起，太太。我闺女正学着当歌唱家。这会儿在意大利呢，卡罗琳（Car'line）在那儿，那花销可了不得。我东抠抠西攒攒弄来的钱，差不多全搭进去了。」

===Original===
There was a little colour in Selina’s face now. “Italy! Oh, Mr. Talcott!” You’d have thought she had seen it, from her face. She began to thank him, gravely.
===Chinese===
塞莉娜脸上这才有了些血色。「意大利！哦，塔尔科特先生！」看她那张脸，你会当她是亲眼见过意大利似的。她开始向他道谢，郑重其事地。

===Original===
“Now, that’s all right, Mis’ DeJong. I notice your stuff’s bunched kind of extry, and all of a size. Fixin’ to do that way right along?”
===Chinese===
「行了行了，不碍事，德容太太。我瞧你这菜捆得格外用心，个头还都一般齐。往后打算一直这么弄？」

===Original===
“Yes. I thought﻿—they looked prettier that way﻿—of course vegetables aren’t supposed to look pretty, I expect﻿—” she stammered, stopped.
===Chinese===
「是。我想——这样它们好看些——当然，蔬菜原是不该讲究好看，我想——」她结结巴巴，住了口。

===Original===
“You fix ’em pretty like that and bring ’em in to me first thing, or send ’em. My trade, they like their stuff kind of special. Yessir.”
===Chinese===
「你照这么拾掇得漂漂亮亮的，头一件事就往我这儿送，或者捎来。我的主顾，就好这一口与众不同。可不。」

===Original===
As she gathered up the reins he stood again in his doorway, cool, remote, his unlighted cigar in his mouth, while hand-trucks rattled past him, barrels and boxes thumped to the sidewalk in front of him, wheels and hoofs and shouts made a great clamour all about him.
===Chinese===
她拢起缰绳的当儿，他又站回了门洞里，冷静，超然，嘴里叼着那支没点着的雪茄；手推车在他身边咔啦啦驶过，木桶和箱子在他面前的人行道上咚咚落地，车轮、马蹄和吆喝在他四下里闹成一片大喧哗。

===Original===
“We going home now?” demanded Dirk. “We going home now? I’m hungry.”
===Chinese===
「咱们这就回家了？」德克追问。「咱们回家了吗？我饿了。」

===Original===
“Yes, lamb.” Two dollars in her pocket. All yesterday’s grim toil, and all today’s, and months of labour behind those two days. Two dollars in the pocket of her black calico petticoat. “We’ll get something to eat when we drive out a ways. Some milk and bread and cheese.”
===Chinese===
「回了，小羊羔。」兜里两块钱。昨天一整天的苦熬苦作，加上今天的，再加上这两天背后几个月的辛劳。两块钱，装在她黑布衬裙的兜里。「赶出一程咱们就买点儿吃的。牛奶、面包，再买点儿奶酪。」

===Original===
The sun was very hot. She took the boy’s hat off, passed her tender work-calloused hand over the damp hair that clung to his forehead. “It’s been fun, hasn’t it?” she said. “Like an adventure. Look at all the kind people we’ve met. Mr. Spanknoebel, and Mr. Talcott﻿—”
===Chinese===
太阳很毒。她摘下孩子的帽子，用她那双做活了生茧却依然温柔的手，抚过他黏在前额上的湿头发。「这一路挺好玩儿的，是不是？」她说。「像一场探险。瞧咱们一路遇见的这些好人。斯潘克诺贝尔先生，还有塔尔科特先生——」

===Original===
“And Mabel.”
===Chinese===
「还有玛贝尔。」

===Original===
Startled, “And Mabel.”
===Chinese===
她一怔。「还有玛贝尔。」

===Original===
She wanted suddenly to kiss him, knew he would hate it with all the boy and all the Holland Dutch in him, and did not.
===Chinese===
她忽然想亲他一下，可她知道，他那一身的男孩劲儿、一身的荷兰脾气都会嫌恶这个，便没有亲。

===Original===
She made up her mind to drive east and then south. Pervus had sometimes achieved a late sale to outlying grocers. Jan’s face if she came home with half the load still on the wagon! And what of the unpaid bills? She had, perhaps, thirty dollars, all told. She owed four hundred. More than that. There were seedlings that Pervus had bought in April to be paid for at the end of the growing season, in the fall. And now fall was here.
===Chinese===
她打定主意先往东，再往南。珀维斯从前有时能把菜迟一步卖给外围的杂货铺。她要是拉着半车菜回家，想想扬那张脸！再说那些没结的账呢？她统共，兴许，有三十块钱。她欠着四百。还不止。四月里珀维斯进的菜秧，讲好到生长季末、入秋结账。如今秋就在眼前了。

===Original===
Fear shook her. She told herself she was tired, nervous. That terrible week. And now this. The heat. Soon they’d be home, she and Dirk. How cool and quiet the house would seem. The squares of the kitchen tablecloth. Her own neat bedroom with the black walnut bed and dresser. The sofa in the parlour with the ruffled calico cover. The old chair on the porch with the cane seat sagging where warp and woof had become loosened with much use and stuck out in ragged tufts. It seemed years since she had seen all this. The comfort of it, the peace of it. Safe, desirable, suddenly dear. No work for a woman, this. Well, perhaps they were right.
===Chinese===
恐惧把她攫住了。她对自己说，她不过是累了，心里发慌。可怕的那一个礼拜。如今又是这一桩。还有这暑热。很快就到家了，她和德克。家里该显得多凉快、多清净。厨房桌布上的一格一格。她自己那间齐整卧室，黑胡桃木的床和衣柜。客厅里那张罩着皱褶细布套的沙发。廊子上那把旧椅子，藤条坐面经纬磨松了，一条条毛茬子翘出来，坐面塌了下去。她仿佛已有多年没见到这一切了。那份熨帖，那份安宁。安全，可意，忽然间又倍感亲爱。这确实不是女人干的活儿。唉，兴许他们是对的。

===Original===
Down Wabash Avenue, with the L trains thundering overhead and her horses, frightened and uneasy with the unaccustomed roar and clangour of traffic, stepping high and swerving stiffly, grotesque and angular in their movements. A dowdy farm woman and a sunburned boy in a rickety vegetable wagon absurdly out of place in this canyon of cobblestones, shops, streetcars, drays, carriages, bicycles, pedestrians. It was terribly hot.
===Chinese===
下了瓦巴什大道（Wabash Avenue），高架列车（the L）在头顶上轰隆隆碾过，她那两匹马叫这不惯的车马轰响震得又惊又躁，蹄子抬得老高，硬邦邦地闪躲，动作笨拙而生硬。一个寒碜的农妇和一个晒黑了的男孩，坐一辆破破烂烂的菜车，在这条鹅卵石、铺面、街车、载重马车、私家马车、自行车和行人构成的峡谷里，格格不入得近乎荒唐。热得要命。

===Original===
The boy’s eyes popped with excitement and bewilderment.
===Chinese===
这孩子的眼睛兴奋得、也发懵得直往外瞪。

===Original===
“Pretty soon,” Selina said. The muscles showed white beneath the skin of her jaw. “Pretty soon. Prairie Avenue. Great big houses, and lawns, all quiet.” She even managed a smile.
===Chinese===
「快到了，」塞莉娜说。她下颌的肌肉在皮底下绷出白色。「快到了。草原大道。老大老大的房子，还有草坪，全都安安静静。」她居然还挤出了一个笑。

===Original===
“I like it better home.”
===Chinese===
「我还是觉着家里好。」

===Original===
Prairie Avenue at last, turning in at Sixteenth Street. It was like calm after a storm. Selina felt battered, spent.
===Chinese===
草原大道终于到了，在十六街口拐了进去。好比风暴过后的一片宁静。塞莉娜只觉得自己被捶打得没了样，力气也使尽了。

===Original===
There were groceries near Eighteenth, and at the other cross-streets﻿—Twenty-second, Twenty-sixth, Thirty-first, Thirty-fifth. They were passing the great stone houses of Prairie Avenue of the ’90s. Turrets and towers, cornices and cupolas, humpbacked conservatories, porte-cochères, bow windows﻿—here lived Chicago’s rich that had made their riches in pork and wheat and dry goods; the selling of necessities to a city that clamoured for them.
===Chinese===
十八街左近有几家杂货铺，其余各条横街上也有一——二十二街、二十六街、三十一街、三十五街。他们正走过九十年代草原大道的那些石头大宅。角楼与塔楼，飞檐与穹顶，驼了背的暖房，通车门廊，凸肚窗——芝加哥的有钱人住在这儿，他们的钱财置在猪肉、麦子和干货上；置在把日用品卖给一座为它们嚷破了口的城市上。

===Original===
“Just like me,” Selina thought, humorously. Then another thought came to her. Her vegetables, canvas covered, were fresher than those in the nearby markets. Why not try to sell some of them here, in these big houses? In an hour she might earn a few dollars this way at retail prices slightly less than those asked by the grocers of the neighbourhood.
===Chinese===
「跟我一个样，」塞莉娜想，心里觉得好笑。跟着另一个念头来了。她的菜苫着帆布，比左近铺子里的还水灵。何不在这儿试试，到这些大宅子里卖掉一些？一个钟头里，按比左近杂货铺稍低些的零售价，兴许就能挣下几块钱。

===Original===
She stopped her wagon in the middle of the block on Twenty-fourth Street. Agilely she stepped down the wheel, gave the reins to Dirk. The horses were no more minded to run than the wooden steeds on a carrousel. She filled a large market basket with the finest and freshest of her stock and with this on her arm looked up a moment at the house in front of which she had stopped. It was a four-story brownstone, with a hideous high stoop. Beneath the steps were a little vestibule and a door that was the tradesmen’s entrance. The kitchen entrance, she knew, was by way of the alley at the back, but this she would not take. Across the sidewalk, down a little flight of stone steps, into the vestibule under the porch. She looked at the bell﻿—a brass knob. You pulled it out, shoved it in, and there sounded a jangling down the dim hallway beyond. Simple enough. Her hand was on the bell. “Pull it!” said the desperate Selina. “I can’t! I can’t!” cried all the prim dim Vermont Peakes, in chorus. “All right. Starve to death and let them take the farm and Dirk, then.”
===Chinese===
她在二十四街那段街的中段停了车。她手脚轻捷地顺着车轮跳下来，把缰绳交给德克。这两匹马要跑的意思，比旋转木马上的木头马多不出一丝一毫。她挑车上最好最新鲜的菜装满一只大提篮，挎在臂弯，抬眼端详了一忽儿她停在这座宅子。这是一座四层楼的褐石宅子，带一道丑得扎眼的高门阶。门阶底下有一小间门厅和一扇门，那是送货人进出的入口。厨房的门，她知道，得绕后头小巷进去，可她偏不走那个。横过人行道，走下一小截石阶，进了廊子底下的小门厅。她端详那只门铃——一个黄铜旋钮。你把它拔出来，再推进去，昏暗的门厅深处便叮零零响起一串。够简单的了。她的手已经搭上铃钮。「拉呀！」走投无路的塞莉娜说。「我不敢！我不敢！」那一族拘谨黯淡的佛蒙特皮克家（Vermont Peakes），齐声喊道。「那好。那就饿死去，让他们把农庄收走，把德克也收走，拉倒。」

===Original===
At that she pulled the knob hard. Jangle went the bell in the hall. Again. Again.
===Chinese===
听了这话，她狠狠一拉旋钮。门厅里铃声叮零。又是一串。又一串。

===Original===
Footsteps up the hall. The door opened to disclose a large woman, high cheek-boned, in a work apron; a cook, apparently.
===Chinese===
门厅里响起脚步声。门开了，现出一个高大的女人，高颧骨，系一条干活围裙；看样子是个厨娘。

===Original===
“Good morning,” said Selina. “Would you like some fresh country vegetables?”
===Chinese===
「早上好，」塞莉娜说。「要一点儿乡下带来的新鲜蔬菜吗？」

===Original===
“No.” She half shut the door, opening it again to ask, “Got any fresh eggs or butter?” At Selina’s negative she closed the door, bolted it. Selina, standing there, basket on arm, could hear her heavy tread down the passageway toward the kitchen. Well, that was all right. Nothing so terrible about that, Selina told herself. Simply hadn’t wanted any vegetables. The next house. The next house, and the next, and the next. Up one side of the street, and down the other. Four times she refilled her basket. At one house she sold a quarter’s worth. Fifteen at another. Twenty cents here. Almost fifty there. “Good morning,” she always said at the door in her clear, distinct way. They stared, usually. But they were curious, too, and did not often shut the door in her face.
===Chinese===
「不要。」她把门半掩上，又开开来问：「有新鲜鸡蛋或黄油吗？」塞莉娜说没有，她便关上门，上了闩。塞莉娜站在那儿，臂上挎着篮子，听得见她沉重的脚步顺着过道朝厨房去了。唔，这没什么。这也没什么了不得的可怕，塞莉娜对自己说。不过是不要菜罢了。下一家。下一家，再下一家，又下一家。街这边走上去，那边走下来。篮子她重新装过四回。有一家她卖了两毛五的菜。另一家一毛五。这家两毛。那家差不多五毛。「早上好，」每回在门口，她总这么清清楚楚、字字分明地说。她们多半会瞪眼瞧她。可她们也好奇，并不常当面把门摔上。

===Original===
“Do you know of a good place?” one kitchen maid said. “This place ain’t so good. She only pays me three dollars. You can get four now. Maybe you know a lady wants a good girl.”
===Chinese===
「您知道有哪家好人家吗？」一个厨房小丫头说。「这家不怎么样。她才给我三块钱。如今外头能挣四块的。兴许您认得哪位太太要寻个好使唤的丫头。」

===Original===
“No,” Selina answered. “No.”
===Chinese===
「不知道，」塞莉娜答道。「不知道。」

===Original===
At another house the cook had offered her a cup of coffee, noting the white face, the look of weariness. Selina refused it, politely. Twenty-first Street﻿—Twenty-fifth﻿—Twenty-eighth. She had over four dollars in her purse. Dirk was weary now and hungry to the point of tears. “The last house,” Selina promised him, “the very last one. After this one we’ll go home.” She filled her basket again. “We’ll have something to eat on the way, and maybe you’ll go to sleep with the canvas over you, high, fastened to the seat like a tent. And we’ll be home in a jiffy.”
===Chinese===
又有一家，厨娘瞧她脸白、一脸倦色，请她喝一杯咖啡。塞莉娜客气地辞了。二十一街——二十五街——二十八街。她钱包里已有四块多钱。德克这会儿又乏又饿，饿得直要哭。「最后一户，」塞莉娜向他许诺，「真真是最后一户。这一户走了咱们就回家。」她又把篮子装满。「路上咱们买点儿吃的，兴许你钻到帆布底下就睡着了，帆布高高地罩下来，拴在座位上，像个帐篷。一眨眼咱们就到家了。」

===Original===
The last house was a new gray stone one, already beginning to turn dingy from the smoke of the Illinois Central suburban trains that puffed along the lake front a block to the east. The house had large bow windows, plump and shining. There was a lawn, with statues, and a conservatory in the rear. Real lace curtains at the downstairs windows with plush hangings behind them. A high iron grille ran all about the property giving it an air of aloofness, of security. Selina glanced at this wrought-iron fence. And it seemed to bar her out. There was something forbidding about it﻿—menacing. She was tired, that was it. The last house. She had almost five dollars, earned in the last hour. “Just five minutes,” she said to Dirk, trying to make her tone bright, her voice gay. Her arms full of vegetables which she was about to place in the basket at her feet she heard at her elbow:
===Chinese===
最后一户是一座新起的灰石宅子，已经叫伊利诺伊中央（Illinois Central）郊区列车喷出的煤烟熏得开始发暗——那列车就在东边一个街区外，沿着湖滨哐嗤哐嗤地驶过。宅子有很大的凸肚窗，饱满锃亮。有一片草坪，草坪上立着雕像，宅后有一座暖房。楼下窗子挂着真花边窗帘，后头衬着长毛绒的帷幔。一道高铁栅栏把整处产业圈了个遍，给它添了一股拒人、一股森严的气派。塞莉娜扫了这道熟铁栅栏一眼。它仿佛把她挡在了外头。这栅栏有点儿不叫人亲近的东西——近乎威吓。她是乏了，就是这么回事。最后一户。这一个钟头里，她挣下了差不多五块钱。「就五分钟，」她对德克说，竭力叫口气轻快、嗓音欢畅。她两臂抱满了菜，正要放进脚边的篮子，肘边响起一句话：

===Original===
“Now, then, where’s your license?”
===Chinese===
「喂，你的执照呢？」

===Original===
She turned. A policeman at her side. She stared up at him. How enormously tall, she thought; and how red his face. “License?”
===Chinese===
她转过身。身边一个警察。她仰起脸瞪着他。个子真是高得吓人，她想；脸也真红。「执照？」

===Original===
“Yeh, you heard me. License. Where’s your peddler’s license? You got one, I s’pose.”
===Chinese===
「对，你听见了。执照。你的小贩执照在哪儿？我想你总有一样儿吧。」

===Original===
“Why, no. No.” She stared at him, still.
===Chinese===
「哦，没有。没有。」她还在盯着他瞧。

===Original===
His face grew redder. Selina was a little worried about him. She thought, stupidly, that if it grew any redder﻿—
===Chinese===
他的脸更红了。塞莉娜倒有点儿替他担忧。她傻乎乎地想，那脸要是再红下去——

===Original===
“Well, say, where d’ye think you are, peddlin’ without a license! A good mind to run you in. Get along out of here, you and the kid. Leave me ketch you around here again!”
===Chinese===
「嘿，我说，你当这是哪儿呢，没执照就敢沿街叫卖！真想把你抓进局子去。带着孩子滚出这一带。再叫我在这儿逮着你们试试！」

===Original===
“What’s the trouble, Officer?” said a woman’s voice. A smart open carriage of the type known as a victoria, with two chestnut horses whose harness shone with metal. Spanking, was the word that came to Selina’s mind, which was acting perversely certainly; crazily. A spanking team. The spankers disdainfully faced Selina’s comic bony nags which were grazing the close-cropped grass that grew in the neat little lawn-squares between curb and sidewalk. “What’s the trouble, Reilly?”
===Chinese===
「怎么回事，警官？」一个女人声音说。一辆时新的敞篷马车，就是那一款唤作维多利亚式（victoria）的车型，两匹栗色马，马具上的金属件锃光瓦亮。神骏——这是浮上塞莉娜心头的词，她这脑子眼下准是存心捣乱；是发疯。好一对神骏。这对神骏鄙夷地正对着塞莉娜那两匹逗笑的瘦骨头马——它们正低着头，啃路缘与人行道之间一小方一小方齐整草坪上剪得极短的青草。「怎么回事，赖利（Reilly）？」

===Original===
The woman stepped out of the victoria. She wore a black silk Eton suit, very modish, and a black hat with a plume.
===Chinese===
那女人从马车上下来。她穿一套黑绸的伊顿套装（Eton suit），十分时新，头上是插着羽饰的黑帽子。

===Original===
“Woman peddling without a license, Mrs. Arnold. You got to watch ’em like a hawk.﻿ ﻿… Get along wid you, then.” He put a hand on Selina’s shoulder and gave her a gentle push.
===Chinese===
「一个女人没执照沿街卖货，阿诺德太太。这号人您得像老鹰似的盯着。……你也走你的吧。」他把一只手放到塞莉娜肩上，轻轻推了她一把。

===Original===
There shook Selina from head to foot such a passion, such a storm of outraged sensibilities, as to cause street, victoria, silk-clad woman, horses, and policeman to swim and shiver in a haze before her eyes. The rage of a fastidious woman who had had an alien male hand put upon her. Her face was white. Her eyes glowed black, enormous. She seemed tall, majestic even.
===Chinese===
塞莉娜浑身上下掀起了那样一股怒火，那样一场尊严受辱的风暴，震得街道、马车、绸衣女人、马匹和警察都在她眼前的一片雾里晃动、发颤。那是一个娇矜自重的女人，被一只外道男人的手搭上身来的怒火。她的脸白了。她的眼睛烧成黑的，胀得老大。她仿佛高大了，甚至是威严了。

===Original===
“Take your hand off me!” Her speech was clipped, vibrant. “How dare you touch me! How dare you! Take your hand!﻿—” The blazing eyes in the white mask. He took his hand from her shoulder. The red surged into her face. A tanned weather-beaten toil-worn woman, her abundant hair skewered into a knob and held by a long gray-black hairpin, her full skirt grimed with the mud of the wagon wheel, a pair of old side-boots on her slim feet, a grotesquely battered old felt hat (her husband’s) on her head, her arms full of ears of sweet corn, and carrots, and radishes and bunches of beets; a woman with bad teeth, flat breasts﻿—even then Julie had known her by her eyes. And she had stared and then run to her in her silk dress and her plumed hat, crying, “Oh, Selina! My dear! My dear!” with a sob of horror and pity. “My dear!” And had taken Selina, carrots, beets, corn, and radishes in her arms. The vegetables lay scattered all about them on the sidewalk in front of Julie Hempel Arnold’s great stone house on Prairie Avenue. But strangely enough it had been Selina who had done the comforting, patting Julie’s plump silken shoulder and saying, over and over, soothingly, as to a child, “There, there! It’s all right, Julie. It’s all right. Don’t cry. What’s there to cry for! Sh-sh! It’s all right.”
===Chinese===
「把你的手拿开！」她的话一字一板，字字震人。「你怎么敢碰我！你怎么敢！把手拿开！——」白面具上一双冒火的眼睛。他把手从她肩上拿开了。红色涌上她的脸。一个晒黑的、风吹日头的、操劳磨损的女人，一头厚发胡乱绾个髻，别一根长长的灰黑发卡，整幅裙子蹭满车轮的泥，一双纤脚上登着旧偏扣靴，头上扣一顶破得滑稽的旧毡帽（她丈夫的），怀里抱满了甜玉米穗、胡萝卜、小萝卜和一把把甜菜；一个牙齿坏了、胸脯平塌的女人——饶是这般，朱莉还是凭那双眼睛认出了她。她先是呆呆地瞪着，随即穿着绸衣、戴着羽帽朝她奔过来，喊着：「哦，塞莉娜！亲爱的！亲爱的！」一声抽咽，又是惊骇又是怜惜。「亲爱的！」她把塞莉娜连同胡萝卜、甜菜、玉米和小萝卜一把搂进怀里。蔬菜撒了两人一身，滚了一地，撒在草原大道上朱莉·亨佩尔·阿诺德家石头大宅前的人行道上。可是说来奇怪，受安慰的偏是朱莉，倒是塞莉娜在哄她，拍着她那丰满的绸肩膀，一遍又一遍，像哄个孩子：「好了，好了！不碍事的，朱莉。不碍事了。别哭了。有什么可哭的！嘘——嘘！不碍事了。」

===Original===
Julie lifted her head in its modish black plumed hat, wiped her eyes, blew her nose. “Get along with you, do,” she said to Reilly, the policeman, using his very words to Selina. “I’m going to report you to Mr. Arnold, see if I don’t. And you know what that means.”
===Chinese===
朱莉那颗戴着时新黑羽帽的头抬起来，擦擦眼睛，擤擤鼻子。「你也走你的吧，走，」她对警察赖利说，用的正是他对塞莉娜的话。「我要把你报给阿诺德先生，瞧我报不报。你是知道那是什么下场。」

===Original===
“Well, now, Mrs. Arnold, ma’am, I was only doing my duty. How cud I know the lady was a friend of yours. Sure, I﻿—” He surveyed Selina, cart, jaded horses, wilted vegetables. “Well, how *cud* I, now, Mrs. Arnold, ma’am!”
===Chinese===
「嗳，别介，阿诺德太太，夫人，我不过是在尽本分。我哪儿知道这位太太是您的朋友呢。真的，我——」他把塞莉娜、菜车、疲塌的马、蔫了的菜打量了一遍。「可不是，我哪儿*能*知道呢，阿诺德太太，夫人！」

===Original===
“And why not!” demanded Julie with superb unreasonableness. “Why not, I’d like to know. Do get along with you.”
===Chinese===
「怎么就不知道！」朱莉质问道，蛮横得理直气壮。「我倒要问问，凭什么不能。快走你的吧。」

===Original===
He got along, a defeated officer of the law, and a bitter. And now it was Julie who surveyed Selina, cart, Dirk, jaded horses, wilted leftover vegetables. “Selina, whatever in the world! What are you doing with﻿—” She caught sight of Selina’s absurd boots then and she began to cry again. At that Selina’s overwrought nerves snapped and she began to laugh, hysterically. It frightened Julie, that laughter. “Selina, don’t! Come in the house with me. What are you laughing at! Selina!”
===Chinese===
他走他的去了，一名吃了败仗的执法官，还满怀怨气。这会儿轮到朱莉打量塞莉娜、菜车、德克、疲塌的马和蔫了的剩菜了。「塞莉娜，这到底是怎么回事！你拉着一车——」话没说完，她一眼瞧见塞莉娜那双可笑的靴子，又哭起来。塞莉娜绷得过紧的神经到此啪地断了，她歇斯底里地大笑起来。那笑声吓着了朱莉。「塞莉娜，别笑了！跟我进屋去。你笑什么呀！塞莉娜！」

===Original===
With shaking finger Selina was pointing at the vegetables that lay tumbled at her feet. “Do you see that cabbage, Julie? Do you remember how I used to despise Mrs. Tebbitt’s because she used to have boiled cabbage on Monday nights?”
===Chinese===
塞莉娜哆嗦着手指，点着脚边散乱的菜。「你瞧见那棵卷心菜了吗，朱莉？你可记得我从前多嫌弃特比特太太那儿——就因为她星期一晚上总吃煮卷心菜？」

===Original===
“That’s nothing to laugh at, is it? Stop laughing this minute, Selina Peake!”
===Chinese===
「那没什么可笑的，对不对？这会儿就给我住笑，塞莉娜·皮克！」

===Original===
“I’ll stop. I’ve stopped now. I was just laughing at my ignorance. Sweat and blood and health and youth go into every cabbage. Did you know that, Julie? One doesn’t despise them as food, knowing that.﻿ ﻿… Come, climb down, Dirk. Here’s a lady mother used to know﻿—oh, years and years ago, when she was a girl. Thousands of years ago.”
===Chinese===
「我停。这不就停了。我方才是在笑我自己有眼无珠。汗水、血、健康和青春，都进了每一棵卷心菜。这个你知道吗，朱莉？知道了这个，就不会再把它们当吃食小看了。……来，下来吧，德克。这位太太是妈妈从前认识的——哦，好多年好多年以前，妈妈还是个姑娘那时候。几千年以前。」

## XII / 第十二章

===Original===
The best thing for Dirk. The best thing for Dirk. It was the phrase that repeated itself over and over in Selina’s speech during the days that followed. Julie Arnold was all for taking him into her gray stone house, dressing him like Lord Fauntleroy and sending him to the north-side private school attended by Eugene, her boy, and Pauline, her girl. In this period of bewilderment and fatigue Julie had attempted to take charge of Selina much as she had done a dozen years before at the time of Simeon Peake’s dramatic death. And now, as then, she pressed into service her wonder-working father and bounden slave, August Hempel. Her husband she dismissed with affectionate disregard.
===Chinese===
对德克最好的事。对德克最好的事。随后那些天里，这句话在塞莉娜的言谈间翻来覆去、念了又念。朱莉·阿诺德一心主张把孩子接进她那座灰石砌的宅子，把他打扮成方特勒罗伊小爵爷（Lord Fauntleroy）的模样，送进城北那家私立学校去——她的儿子尤金、女儿波琳上的都是那里。在这段失魂落魄、疲惫不堪的日子里，朱莉试图全盘照管塞莉娜，一如十二年前西缅·皮克轰然死去时她干的那样。于是同当年一样，她又把自己那位无所不能的父亲兼心甘情愿的奴仆奥古斯特·亨佩尔征调出来。至于丈夫，她则带着亲昵的漫不经心，把他撇在了一边。
===Original===
“Michael’s all right,” she had said on that day of their first meeting, “if you tell him what’s to be done. He’ll always do it. But Pa’s the one that thinks of things.
