He’s like a general, and Michael’s the captain. Well, now, Pa’ll be out tomorrow and I’ll probably come with him. I’ve got a committee meeting, but I can easily﻿—”
===Chinese===
「迈克尔人没问题，」她们重逢那天朱莉这么说过，「只要你告诉他该做什么。他总会照办。可肯动脑子想办法的是爸爸。他是将军，迈克尔不过是上尉。哎，这么着，爸爸明天就过去，我多半也一起来。我还有个委员会要开会，不过我可以很容易——」
===Original===
“You said﻿—did you say your father would be out tomorrow! Out where?”
===Chinese===
「你说——你是说你父亲明天要过来！过来哪儿？」
===Original===
“To your place. Farm.”
===Chinese===
「到你那儿去。农场。」
===Original===
“But why should he? It’s a little twenty-five-acre truck farm, and half of it under water a good deal of the time.”
===Chinese===
「可他去干什么呀？那不过是二十五英亩大小的一片菜园子，倒有一半地常常泡在水里。」
===Original===
“Pa’ll find a use for it, never fear. He won’t say much, but he’ll think of things. And then everything will be all right.”
===Chinese===
「爸爸总能给它派上用场，放心好了。他话不多，可他有主意。到那时一切就都顺当了。」
===Original===
“It’s miles. Miles. Way out in High Prairie.”
===Chinese===
「隔着多少里地呢。多少里地。远在高草原乡下呢。」
===Original===
“Well, if you could make it with those horses, Selina, I guess we can with Pa’s two grays that hold a record for a mile in three minutes or three miles in a minute, I forget which. Or in the auto, though Pa hates it. Michael is the only one in the family who likes it.”
===Chinese===
「嗐，塞莉娜，你赶着那两匹马都走得下来，爸爸那两匹灰马更不在话下——它们保着一项纪录，一英里三分钟，要不就是一分钟三英里，我记不清是哪一桩了。再不就坐汽车去，虽说爸爸见不得那玩意儿。全家就迈克尔一个人喜欢它。」
===Original===
A species of ugly pride now possessed Selina. “I don’t need help. Really I don’t, Julie dear. It’s never been like today. Never before. We were getting on very well, Pervus and I. Then after Pervus’s death so suddenly like that I was frightened. Terribly frightened. About Dirk. I wanted him to have everything. Beautiful things. I wanted his life to be beautiful. Life can be so ugly, Julie. You don’t know. You don’t know.”
===Chinese===
此刻一种难看的自尊攫住了塞莉娜。「我不需要人帮。真不需要，亲爱的朱莉。从没有像今天这样过。从前没有过。我和珀维斯过得好好的，一直好好的。后来珀维斯那样突然一死，我害怕了。怕极了。为的是德克。我要他什么都有。美的东西。我要他的一生是美的。日子能有多丑，朱莉，你是不知道。你不知道。」
===Original===
“Well, now, that’s why I say. We’ll be out tomorrow, Pa and I. Dirk’s going to have everything beautiful. We’ll see to that.”
===Chinese===
「哎，所以我才这么说呀。明天我和爸爸就过去。德克会有一切美的东西。这个包在我们身上。」
===Original===
It was then that Selina had said, “But that’s just it. I want to do it myself, for him. I can. I want to give him all these things myself.”
===Chinese===
就在这时塞莉娜说了：「可症结就在这儿。这件事我要亲手来做，为了他。我做得来。我要亲手把这一切给了他。」
===Original===
“But that’s selfish.”
===Chinese===
「可这就自私了。」
===Original===
“I don’t mean to be. I just want to do the best thing for Dirk.”
===Chinese===
「我不是存心自私。我只想做那件对德克最好的事。」
===Original===
It was shortly after noon that High Prairie, hearing the unaccustomed chug of a motor, rushed to its windows or porches to behold Selina DeJong in her mashed black felt hat and Dirk waving his battered straw wildly, riding up the Halsted road toward the DeJong farm in a bright red automobile that had shattered the nerves of every farmer’s team it had met on the way. Of the DeJong team and the DeJong dog Pom, and the DeJong vegetable wagon there was absolutely no sign. High Prairie was rendered unfit for work throughout the next twenty-four hours.
===Chinese===
刚过晌午，高草原上听得不习见的汽车嘟嘟声，家家赶着涌到窗口或廊子前，只见塞莉娜·德容戴着她那顶压瘪的黑毡帽，德克发疯似的挥着他那顶破草帽，乘一辆通红的汽车，沿哈尔斯特德路直奔德容农场而来——这一路上，凡它遇上的农家马队，无不叫它惊得魂飞魄散。至于德容家的马、德容家的狗波姆、德容家的菜车，则半点影儿也不见。往后整整二十四小时，高草原全然缓不过神来干活了。
===Original===
The idea had been Julie’s, and Selina had submitted rather than acquiesced, for by now she was too tired to combat anything or anyone. If Julie had proposed her entering High Prairie on the back of an elephant with a mahout perched between his ears Selina would have agreed﻿—rather, would have been unable to object.
===Chinese===
这主意是朱莉出的。塞莉娜是屈从，不是心许——她如今已经累得无力跟任何事、任何人较量了。哪怕朱莉提议让她骑在大象背上进高草原，两只象耳朵中间还跨坐着个象夫（mahout），塞莉娜也会答应——倒不如说，将无从反对。
===Original===
“It’ll get you home in no time,” Julie had said, energetically. “You look like a ghost and the boy’s half asleep. I’ll telephone Pa and he’ll have one of the men from the barns drive your team out so it’ll be there by six. Just you leave it all to me. Haven’t you ever ridden in one! Why, there’s nothing to be scared of. I like the horses best, myself. I’m like Pa. He says if you use horses you get there.”
===Chinese===
「一眨眼就送你到家了，」朱莉曾这样说，劲头十足。「你瞧着活像个鬼，孩子也半睡半醒的。我给爸爸打电话，叫他打发牲口棚里一个人把你的马队赶过去，六点前准到。你只管把一切都交给我。你从来没坐过汽车吧！嗐，这有什么可怕的。要叫我说，我还是顶喜欢马。我随爸爸。他说，使唤马，你总归到得了。」
===Original===
Dirk had accepted the new conveyance with the adaptability of childhood, had even predicted, grandly, “I’m going to have one when I grow up that’ll go faster ’n this, even.”
===Chinese===
德克拿出小孩子随遇而安的本事，接受了这新式代步家伙，甚至还大言不惭地预言：「等我长大了，也要有一辆，跑得比这辆还快。」
===Original===
“Oh, you wouldn’t want to go faster than this, Dirk,” Selina had protested breathlessly as they chugged along at the alarming rate of almost fifteen miles an hour.
===Chinese===
「哦，你总不想比这更快了吧，德克。」汽车以每小时将近十五英里这骇人的速度突突前行，塞莉娜气都提不上来地抗议道。
===Original===
Jan Snip had been rendered speechless. Until the actual arrival of the team and wagon at six he counted them as mysteriously lost and DeJong’s widow clearly gone mad. August Hempel’s arrival next day with Julie seated beside him in the light spider-phaeton drawn by two slim wild-eyed quivering grays made little tumult in Jan’s stunned mind by now incapable of absorbing any fresh surprises.
===Chinese===
扬·斯尼普给惊得哑口无言。直到六点钟马队和菜车当真到了家，他都认定它们是神秘失踪了，德容家的寡妇分明是疯了。第二天奥古斯特·亨佩尔驾到——朱莉坐在他身边，那辆轻巧的蜘蛛式四轮马车（spider-phaeton）由两匹精瘦、瞪圆了眼、瑟瑟发抖的灰马拉着——在扬那颗已经震麻、再也容不下任何新鲜惊吓的脑袋里，这也没掀起多大动静。
===Original===
In the twelve years’ transition from butcher to packer Aug Hempel had taken on a certain authority and distinction. Now, at fifty-five, his hair was gray, relieving the too-ruddy colour of his face. He talked almost without an accent; used the idiomatic American speech he heard about the yards, where the Hempel packing plant was situated. Only his *d*’s were likely to sound like *t*’s. The letter *j* had a slightly *ch* sound. In the last few years he had grown very deaf in one ear, so that when you spoke to him he looked at you intently. This had given him a reputation for keenness and great character insight, when it was merely the protective trick of a man who does not want to confess that he is hard of hearing. He wore square-toed shoes with soft tips and square-cut gray clothes and a large gray hat with a chronically inadequate sweatband. The square-cut boots were expensive, and the square-cut gray clothes and the large gray hat, but in them he always gave the effect of being dressed in the discarded garments of a much larger man.
===Chinese===
从肉铺掌柜到肉品加工商，这十二年一变，奥格·亨佩尔身上有了几分威严气派。如今五十五岁，头发花白了，倒把那张脸衬得不那么红得刺眼。他说话几乎不带乡音了；满口是他在场子那一带听来的地道美国话——亨佩尔家的加工厂就设在那里。只是他的 *d* 音听着总像 *t*，字母 *j* 又略带 *ch* 的味儿。近几年他一只耳朵背得厉害，因此你同他说话时，他总是目不转睛地端详你。这倒给他挣来一个头脑敏锐、极善察人的名声，其实那不过是一个不肯承认自己耳背的人自护的巧招。他脚蹬方头软面皮鞋，身穿方裁剪的灰衣裳，头上一顶灰色大帽子，帽里的汗带永远不中用。那双方头靴价钱不菲，那身方裁剪灰衣裳和灰色大帽子也一样，可他往里一装，总像是套着另一个大得多的男人淘汰下来的旧衣裳。
===Original===
Selina’s domain he surveyed with a keen and comprehensive eye.
===Chinese===
塞莉娜这份基业，他用一双敏锐而周全的眼睛视察了一遍。
===Original===
“You want to sell?”
===Chinese===
「你想卖？」
===Original===
“No.”
===Chinese===
「不。」
===Original===
“That’s good.” (It was nearly goot as he said it.) “Few years from now this land will be worth money.” He had spent a bare fifteen minutes taking shrewd valuation of the property from fields to barn, from barn to house. “Well, what *do* you want to do, heh, Selina?”
===Chinese===
「这就好。」（从他嘴里说出来，几乎是「古特」〔goot〕——他的 *d* 一律念作 *t*。）「过不了几年，这地就值钱了。」他统共花了十五分钟，便把这份产业从田到棚、从棚到屋精明地估完了价。「那么，你究竟想*干*什么，嗯，塞莉娜？」
===Original===
They were seated in the cool and unexpectedly pleasing little parlour, with its old Dutch lustre set gleaming softly in the cabinet, its three rows of books, its air of comfort and usage.
===Chinese===
几个人坐在那间凉快、出人意料地讨人喜欢的小客厅里：柜子里那套老荷兰虹彩陶器泛着柔光，三排书，一派安适受用的气象。
===Original===
Dirk was in the yard with one of the Van Ruys boys, surveying the grays proprietorially. Jan was rooting in the fields. Selina clasped her hands tightly in her lap﻿—those hands that, from much grubbing in the soil, had taken on something of the look of the gnarled things they tended. The nails were short, discoloured, broken. The palms rough, calloused. The whole story of the last twelve years of Selina’s life was written in her two hands.
===Chinese===
德克同范勒斯家（Van Ruys）的一个小子在院子里，正以主人的目光打量那两匹灰马。扬在地里翻土做活。塞莉娜把双手紧紧扣在膝上——这双手因为常年扒弄泥土，已经有些长成了它们所伺候的那些疙瘩盘曲之物的模样。指甲短，变了色，豁着口。掌心粗，满是茧。塞莉娜这十二年的生活史，全都写在她这两只手上。
===Original===
“I want to stay here, and work the farm, and make it pay. I can. By next spring my asparagus is going to begin to bring in money. I’m not going to grow just the common garden stuff any more﻿—not much, anyway. I’m going to specialize in the fine things﻿—the kind the South Water Street commission men want. I want to drain the low land. Tile it. That land hasn’t been used for years. It ought to be rich growing land by now, if once it’s properly drained. And I want Dirk to go to school. Good schools. I never want my son to go to the Haymarket. Never. Never.”
===Chinese===
「我要留在这儿，把农场经营出利来。我做得到。到明年开春，我的芦笋就开始进钱了。大路菜我再也不种了——至少不多种了。我要专种上等货——南水街代卖商们要的那一路。我要把洼地排干。铺瓦管。那块地荒了多少年了。只要排水排得彻底，到如今早该是一等一的沃土了。还有德克，我要他念书。念好学校。我绝不要我儿子上海马克特去。绝不。绝不。」
===Original===
Julie stirred with a little rustle and click of silk and beads. Her gentle amiability was vaguely alarmed by the iron quality of determination in the other’s tone.
===Chinese===
朱莉挪了挪身子，丝绸衣料和珠子饰物发出一阵细碎的窸窣与咔嗒。对方话音里那股铁一样的决绝，隐隐惊动了她的温婉和气。
===Original===
“Yes, but what about you, Selina?”
===Chinese===
「可不是，可你自己怎么办呢，塞莉娜？」
===Original===
“Me?”
===Chinese===
「我？」
===Original===
“Yes, of course. You talk as though you didn’t count. Your life. Things to make you happy.”
===Chinese===
「是啊，这还用说。你说得倒好像你自个儿不算数似的。你的一生。那些叫你快活的东西。」
===Original===
“My life doesn’t count, except as something for Dirk to use. I’m done with anything else. Oh, I don’t mean that I’m discouraged, or disappointed in life, or anything like that. I mean I started out with the wrong idea. I know better now. I’m here to keep Dirk from making the mistakes I made.”
===Chinese===
「我这一生不算数，不过是给德克受用的东西罢了。旁的一切我都交代完了。哦，我不是说我灰了心，或者对生活死了心，不是那么回事。我是说，我起头起错了——抱着个错误的念头起的头。如今我懂了。我在这儿，就是为了不让德克去犯我犯下的错。」
===Original===
Here Aug Hempel, lounging largely in his chair and eyeing Selina intently, turned his gaze absently through the window to where the grays, a living equine statue, stood before the house. His tone was one of meditation, not of argument. “It don’t work out that way, seems. About mistakes it’s funny. You got to make your own; and not only that, if you try to keep people from making theirs they get mad.” He whistled softly through his teeth following this utterance and tapped the chair seat with his finger nails.
===Chinese===
这时奥格·亨佩尔大咧咧地瘫在椅子里，目不转睛地端详塞莉娜，随后又心不在焉地把目光挪向窗外那两匹灰马——一座活生生的马的雕像——立在屋子前头。他的话音是沉吟的口气，不是抬杠。「事情算下来不是那么回事，好像。说到犯错，怪就怪在这儿。自己的错总得自己犯；还不止呢，你若拦着别人不叫人家犯人家的错，人家还要跟你恼。」这番话出口，他从齿缝里轻轻打了个呼哨，指甲笃笃地敲着椅子面。
===Original===
“It’s beauty!” Selina said then, almost passionately. Aug Hempel and Julie plainly could make nothing of this remark so she went on, eager, explanatory. “I used to think that if you wanted beauty﻿—if you wanted it hard enough and hopefully enough﻿—it came to you. You just waited, and lived your life as best you could, knowing that beauty might be just around the corner. You just waited, and then it came.”
===Chinese===
「是美！」塞莉娜这时开了口，几乎是激切的。奥格和朱莉明摆着摸不着这话的头脑，她便接着往下说，又急切，又像解说。「我从前总以为，你若是想要美——想得够狠、盼得够诚——它自己就会来找你。你只管等，只管把日子过得像模像样，心里知道美兴许就在下一个街角上候着你。你只管等，等着等着，它就来了。」
===Original===
“Beauty!” exclaimed Julie, weakly. She stared at Selina in the evident belief that this work-worn haggard woman was bemoaning her lack of personal pulchritude.
===Chinese===
「美！」朱莉失声叫道，有气无力。她直勾勾瞪着塞莉娜，分明以为这个操劳憔悴的女人是在自伤容貌不济。
===Original===
“Yes. All the worthwhile things in life. All mixed up. Rooms in candlelight. Leisure. Colour. Travel. Books. Music. Pictures. People﻿—all kinds of people. Work that you love. And growth﻿—growth and watching people grow. Feeling very strongly about things and then developing that feeling to﻿—to make something fine come of it.” The word self-expression was not in cant use then, and Selina hadn’t it to offer them. They would not have known what she meant if she had. She threw out her hands now in a futile gesture. “That’s what I mean by beauty. I want Dirk to have it.”
===Chinese===
「是啊。人生里一切值得要的东西。全搅和在一处。烛光里的房间。清闲。色彩。旅行。书。音乐。画。人——形形色色的人。你热爱的工作。还有生长——生长，看着人一寸寸长起来。对事物抱有强烈的感受，再把那感受培育开去——培育得结出些美好的东西来。」自我表现（self-expression）这个词当年还不曾行世，塞莉娜拿不出来给他们。便是拿得出来，他们也不会懂得她指什么。这时她双手一摊，一个全然徒劳的手势。「我说的美，就是这个。我要德克有它。」
===Original===
Julie blinked and nodded with the wise amiable look of comprehension assumed by one who has understood no single word of what has been said. August Hempel cleared his throat.
===Chinese===
朱莉眨眨眼，点点头，摆出一副聪明和气的了然神情——一个字也没听明白的人，惯会摆出这种了然神情。奥古斯特·亨佩尔清了清嗓子。
===Original===
“I guess I know what you’re driving at Selina, maybe. About Julie I felt just like that. She should have everything fine. I wanted her to have everything. And she did, too. Cried for the moon she had it.”
===Chinese===
「你的用意，我兴许明白几分，塞莉娜。对朱莉，我就是这个心思。她该有的一切好的，我都想给她。她也真都有了。哭着喊着要月亮，月亮也到手了。」
===Original===
“I never did have it Pa, any such thing!”
===Chinese===
「爸爸，我几时有过这种事，从来没有！」
===Original===
“Never cried for it, I know of.”
===Chinese===
「据我所知，倒从没哭着要过。」
===Original===
“For pity’s sake!” pleaded Julie, the literal, “let’s stop talking and do something. My goodness, anybody with a little money can have books and candles and travel around and look at pictures, if that’s all. So let’s *do* something. Pa, you’ve probably got it all fixed in your mind long ago. It’s time we heard it. Here Selina was one of the most popular girls in Miss Fister’s school, and lots of people thought the prettiest. And now just look at her!”
===Chinese===
「老天爷行行好！」实心眼的朱莉央告道，「咱们别净说空话了，办点正经事吧。我的天，但凡手头有几个钱，谁都能有书、有蜡烛、四处逛逛、看看画，要是总共就这些的话。所以咱们快*办*点什么吧。爸爸，你肚子里八成早就盘算停当了，也该说给我们听了。想当年塞莉娜在菲斯特学校里，数一数二招人喜欢，多少人还说她顶顶漂亮呢。可如今你再瞧瞧她！」
===Original===
A flicker of the old flame leaped up in Selina. “Flatterer!” she murmured.
===Chinese===
旧日的火苗在塞莉娜心里忽地一蹿。「甜嘴甜舌！」她低声道。
===Original===
Aug Hempel stood up. “If you think giving your whole life to making the boy happy is going to make him happy you ain’t so smart as I took you for. You go trying to live somebody else’s life for them.”
===Chinese===
奥格·亨佩尔站起身来。「要是你以为，把整条命搭上去博那孩子快活，他就真的快活了，那你可不如我先前当你那么精明。你去试试替旁人过日子看。」
===Original===
“I’m not going to live his life for him. I want to show him how to live it so that he’ll get full value out of it.”
===Chinese===
「我不要替他过日子。我要教他怎么过，好教他从这一生里取足本该得的价值。」
===Original===
“Keeping him out of the Haymarket if the Haymarket’s the natural place for him to be won’t do that. How can you tell! Monkeying with what’s to be. I’m out at the yards every day, in and out of the cattle pens, talking to the drovers and herders, mixing in with the buyers. I can tell the weight of a hog and what he’s worth just by a look at him, and a steer, too. My son-in-law Michael Arnold sits up in the office all day in our plant, dictating letters. His clothes they never stink of the pens like mine do.﻿ ﻿… Now I ain’t saying anything against him, Julie. But I bet my grandson Eugene”﻿—he repeated it, stressing the name so that you sensed his dislike of it﻿—“Eugene, if he comes into the business at all when he grows up, won’t go within smelling distance of the yards. His office I bet will be in a new office building on, say Madison Street, with a view of the lake. Life! You’ll be hoggin’ it all yourself and not know it.”
===Chinese===
「要是海马克特天生就是他该待的地方，你偏把他拦在外头，那可成不了事。你怎么就知道不是呢！净瞎拨弄将来的事。我天天泡在场子里，牛栏猪圈里进进出出，同赶牲口的、圈牲口的说话，同买主们厮混。一头猪多重、值几个钱，我搭眼一瞅就有数，牛也一样。我女婿迈克尔·阿诺德成天坐我们厂子的写字间里，口述信件。他的衣裳从来不沾圈栏的臊臭，不似我的。……行了，朱莉，我可不是编排他。可我敢打赌，我那外孙尤金」——他把这名字又说了一遍，咬得重重的，叫你觉出他对这名字的嫌恶——「尤金，等他长大，就算进这一行，也断不会走到闻得见场子味儿的地方去。我敢说他的写字间准在一栋新起的大楼里，就说麦迪逊街（Madison Street）吧，还望得见湖。生活！你就要自个儿把它一口独吞了，自己还不知道哩。」
===Original===
“Don’t pay any attention to him,” Julie interposed. “He goes on like that. Old yards!”
===Chinese===
「别理他，」朱莉岔开话头。「他就爱这么念叨。张口闭口老场子！」
===Original===
August Hempel bit off the end of a cigar, was about to spit out the speck explosively, thought better of it and tucked it in his vest pocket. “I wouldn’t change places with Mike, not﻿—”
===Chinese===
奥古斯特·亨佩尔咬下一截雪茄头，正要噗地一声把那点碎屑喷出去，转念又作罢，把它塞进了马甲口袋。「我不跟迈克换这把交椅，不——」
===Original===
“Please don’t call him Mike, Pa.”
===Chinese===
「爸爸，请别管他叫迈克。」
===Original===
“Michael, then. Not for ten million. And I need ten million right now.”
===Chinese===
「那就迈克尔。一千万我也不换。何况眼下的我正缺着一千万呢。」
===Original===
“And I suppose,” retorted Selina, spiritedly, “that when your son-in-law Michael Arnold is your age he’ll be telling Eugene how he roughed it in an office over at the yards in the old days. These will be the old days.”
===Chinese===
「照这么说，」塞莉娜奋然回敬，「等你女婿迈克尔·阿诺德活到你这岁数，也就该给尤金讲古了，讲他当年在场子那边一间写字间里怎么苦挨苦撑来着。今天这些，就要成当年了。」
===Original===
August Hempel laughed good humouredly. “That can be, Selina. That can be.” He chewed his cigar and settled to the business at hand.
===Chinese===
奥古斯特·亨佩尔呵呵地笑了。「有这个可能，塞莉娜。有这个可能。」他嚼着雪茄，把心思落到眼面前的事上来。
===Original===
“You want to drain and tile. Plant high-grade stuff. You got to have a man on the place that knows what’s what, not this Rip Van Winkle we saw in the cabbage field. New horses. A wagon.” His eyes narrowed speculatively. Shrewd wrinkles radiated from their corners. “I betcha we’ll see the day when you truck farmers will run into town with your stuff in big automobile wagons that will get you there in under an hour. It’s bound to come. The horse is doomed, that’s chust what.” Then, abruptly, “I will get you the horses, a bargain, at the yards.” He took out a long flat check book. He began writing in it with a pen that he took from his pocket﻿—some sort of marvellous pen that seemed already filled with ink and that you unscrewed at the top and then screwed at the bottom. He squinted through his cigar smoke, the check book propped on his knee. He tore off the check with a clean rip. “For a starter,” he said. He held it out to Selina.
===Chinese===
「你要排水，要铺瓦管。种上等货色。庄上得有个懂行的把式，不是咱们在卷心菜地里见着的那位瑞普·凡·温克尔。要添新马。要一辆车。」他的眼睛盘算着眯细了，眼角绽开一圈圈精明的皱纹。「我敢保，早晚有那一天，你们这些种菜的开着大汽车车，满载着货进城，不出一个钟头就到了。这一天躲不掉。马是命里注定了要完的，就是这么回事（chust，just 的德裔念法）。」跟着，话锋一转：「马我去替你置办，捡个便宜，场子里就有。」他掏出一本窄长的支票簿，又从口袋里摸出一支笔，就着簿子写起来——一种了不得的笔，仿佛肚里早灌好了墨水，顶上一拧便开、底下一拧便紧的那种。他眯缝着眼，隔着雪茄的烟雾看人，支票簿支在膝头。嚓的一声脆响，他把支票撕了下来。「先拿去起个头，」他说。说着递给塞莉娜。
===Original===
“There now!” exclaimed Julie, in triumphant satisfaction. That was more like it. Doing something.
===Chinese===
「这不结了！」朱莉嚷道，一副得胜般的满足。这才像话。办实事了。
===Original===
But Selina did not take the check. She sat very still in her chair, her hands folded. “That isn’t the regular way,” she said.
===Chinese===
可塞莉娜没有去接支票。她在椅子里端坐不动，两手交叠着。「不兴这么个规矩，」她说。
===Original===
August Hempel was screwing the top on his fountain pen again. “Regular way? for what?”
===Chinese===
奥古斯特·亨佩尔正把自来水笔的笔帽往回拧。「规矩？什么的规矩？」
===Original===
“I’m borrowing this money, not taking it. Oh, yes, I am! I couldn’t get along without it. I realize that now, after yesterday. Yesterday! But in five years﻿—seven﻿—I’ll pay it back.” Then, at a half-uttered protest from Julie, “That’s the only way I’ll take it. It’s for Dirk. But I’m going to earn it﻿—and pay it back. I want a﻿—” she was being enormously businesslike, and unconsciously enjoying it﻿—“a﻿—an I.O.U. A promise to pay you back just as﻿—as soon as I can. That’s business, isn’t it? And I’ll sign it.”
===Chinese===
「这钱我是借，不是受。哦，不，我要借的！没有它，我周转不开。这个我现在才明白，过了昨天才明白。昨天哪！可不出五年——七年——我准还上。」朱莉刚吐出半个不字，她便接下去：「这样我才肯拿。这是为德克。可这钱我要挣出来——还上。我要一张——」她那副正经做买卖的架势煞是可观，自己也浑然不觉受用其中——「一张——一张借据（I.O.U.）。白纸黑字，应下只等我一——一能还便还。这就叫生意，对不对？我来签字。」
===Original===
“Sure,” said Aug Hempel, and unscrewed his fountain pen again. “Sure that’s business.” Very serious, he scribbled again, busily, on a piece of paper. A year later, when Selina had learned many things, among them that simple and compound interest on money loaned are not mere problems devised to fill Duffy’s Arithmetic in her school-teaching days, she went to August Hempel between laughter and tears.
===Chinese===
「行，」奥格·亨佩尔说，又把自来水笔拧开了。「行，这就叫生意。」他一脸郑重，伏在一张纸上，刷刷地又写起来。一年之后，塞莉娜已经学会了许多事，其中一样是：放出去的钱所生的单利与复利，并不单单是她教书那几年为填满达菲《算术》才编出来的题目——她于是笑着、又哭着，去找了奥古斯特·亨佩尔。
===Original===
“You didn’t say one word about interest, that day. Not a word. What a little fool you must have thought me.”
===Chinese===
「那天，利息两个字你提都没提。一个字也没提。你心里不定把我当成了多傻的小傻子。」
===Original===
“Between friends,” protested August Hempel.
===Chinese===
「朋友之间，使不得，」奥古斯特·亨佩尔推辞道。
===Original===
But﻿—“No,” Selina insisted. “Interest.”
===Chinese===
可是——「不，」塞莉娜不让，「要算利息。」
===Original===
“I guess I better start me a bank pretty soon if you keep on so businesslike.”
===Chinese===
「照你这股子生意经劲儿，我看我趁早自个儿开家银行得了。」
===Original===
Ten years later he was actually the controlling power in the Yards & Rangers’ Bank. And Selina had that original I.O.U. with its “Paid In Full. Aug Hempel,” carefully tucked away in the carved oak chest together with other keepsakes that she foolishly treasured﻿—ridiculous scraps that no one but she would have understood or valued﻿—a small school slate such as little children use (the one on which she had taught Pervus to figure and parse); a dried bunch of trilliums; a bustled and panniered wine-red cashmere dress, absurdly old-fashioned; a letter telling about the Infanta Eulalie of Spain, and signed Julie Hempel Arnold; a pair of men’s old side-boots with mud caked on them; a crude sketch, almost obliterated now, done on a torn scrap of brown paper and showing the Haymarket with the wagons vegetable-laden and the men gathered beneath the street-flares, and the patient farm horses﻿—Roelf’s childish sketch.
===Chinese===
十年之后，他当真成了场栏与牧人银行（Yards & Rangers’ Bank）说一不二的掌权人。而塞莉娜把那张原始借据——上面添着「全数收讫。奥格·亨佩尔」——仔细收在雕花橡木箱里，同其他一些她痴心珍藏的念物放在一处——除她之外，谁也不懂、谁也不当玩意儿的一些可笑零碎——一块小孩子写字用的石板（她当年就在上面教珀维斯演算、析句）；一束干透了的延龄草；一条垫腰撑臀、酒红色的开司米裙，式样老得近乎可笑；一封信，讲西班牙欧拉利娅公主的事，落款朱莉·亨佩尔·阿诺德；一双男人的旧偏扣靴，泥块还硬结在上头；一幅粗拙的素描，如今几乎磨得看不清了，画在一片撕破的牛皮纸上——海马克特，满载菜蔬的马车，聚在街灯焰火底下的汉子们，还有那些任劳任怨的农家马——罗尔夫幼年时的手笔。
===Original===
Among this rubbish she rummaged periodically in the years that followed. Indeed, twenty years later Dirk, coming upon her smoothing out the wrinkled yellow creases of the I.O.U. or shaking the camphor-laden folds of the wine-red cashmere, would say, “At it again! What a sentimental generation yours was, Mother. Pressed flowers! They went out with the attic, didn’t they? If the house caught fire you’d probably run for the junk in that chest. It isn’t worth two cents, the lot of it.”
===Chinese===
往后的年月里，她隔三差五便要在这堆破烂中间翻捡一气。果不其然，二十年后，德克撞见她抚平那张借据上发黄的皱褶，或是抖开那件酒红开司米裙上熏透樟脑的褶层，便会说：「又弄上啦！你们那一代人，感伤劲儿可真足，妈妈。压花！早跟阁楼一块儿绝迹了，不是么？房子要是着了火，你八成头一个冲去抢救那口箱子里的破烂。统共不值两分钱，那一堆玩意儿。」
===Original===
“Perhaps not,” Selina said, slowly. “Still, there’d be some money value, I suppose, in an early original signed sketch by Rodin.”
===Chinese===
「兴许不值，」塞莉娜慢悠悠地说。「不过一幅罗丹（Rodin）早年署了名的原稿素描，我想，总还值几个钱吧。」
===Original===
“Rodin! You haven’t got a﻿—”
===Chinese===
「罗丹！你并没有——」
===Original===
“No, but here’s one by Pool﻿—Roelf Pool﻿—signed. At a sale in New York last week one of his sketches﻿—not a finished thing at all﻿—just a rough drawing that he’d made of some figures in a group that went into the Doughboy statue﻿—brought one thous﻿—”
===Chinese===
「没有，可这儿有一幅普尔的——罗尔夫·普尔——署了名的。上礼拜纽约一场拍卖，他的一幅素描——压根算不得完成之作——不过是给一座群像里几个人物起的稿子，后来那座群像做进了步兵雕像（Doughboy）——拍出了一千——」
===Original===
“Oh, well, that﻿—yes. But the rest of the stuff you’ve got there﻿—funny how people will treasure old stuff like that. Useless stuff. It isn’t even beautiful.”
===Chinese===
「哦，那个——那个自然。可你收在里头的其余那些玩意儿——人也真是怪，怎么就这样宝贝这些老物件。没用的东西。连美都谈不上。」
===Original===
“Beautiful!” said Selina, and shut the lid of the old chest. “Why, Dirk﻿—Dirk! You don’t even know what beauty is. You never will know.”
===Chinese===
「美！」塞莉娜说，随手合上了那口旧箱的盖子。「唉，德克——德克！你连美是什么都不知道。你永远也不会知道。」

## XIII / 第十三章

===Original===
If those vague characteristics called (variously) magnetism, manner, grace, distinction, attractiveness, fascination, go to make up that nebulous quality known as charm; and if the possessor of that quality is accounted fortunate in his equipment for that which the class-day orators style the battle of life, then Dirk DeJong was a lucky lad and life lay promisingly before him. Undoubtedly he had it; and undoubtedly it did. People said that things “came easy” for Dirk. He said so himself, not boastfully, but rather shyly. He was not one to talk a great deal. Perhaps that was one of his most charming qualities. He listened so well. And he was so quietly effortless. He listened while other people talked, his fine head inclined just a little to one side and bent toward you. Intent on what you were saying, and evidently impressed by it. You felt him immensely intelligent, appreciative. It was a gift more valuable than any other social talent he might have possessed. He himself did not know how precious an attribute this was to prove in a later day when to be allowed to finish a sentence was an experience all too rare. Older men especially said he was a smart young feller and would make his mark. This, surprisingly enough, after a conversation to which he had contributed not a word other than “Yes,” or “No,” or, “Perhaps you’re right, sir,” in the proper places.

===Chinese===
如果说那些笼统唤作魅力、风度、雅致、气派、讨喜、迷人的种种含糊品性，合起来构成了那件名为「魅力」（charm）的缥缈之物；如果说身怀此物者，在毕业典礼演说家们所谓的人生之战中算得装备精良、得天独厚，那么德克·德容便是个幸运的小伙子，前程似锦地铺展在他面前。毋庸置疑，他有这东西；也毋庸置疑，这东西管用。人们说，事事到德克手里都「来得容易」。他自己也这么说，不带吹嘘，倒带着几分腼腆。他不是个多话的人。这兴许正是他最迷人的品性之一。他多么会听人说话。又是多么从容不费力。别人说话时他听着，那颗清秀的头微微偏向一侧，朝你倾过来。专注于你说的每句话，而且显然为之所动。你觉出他绝顶聪明、善解人意。这是一份比他可能拥有的任何其他社交天赋都更可贵的才能。日后，当「容你把一句话说完」成了再难得不过的经历时，这禀赋该何等珍贵——他自己当时却并不知道。年长的人尤其说他是精明的小伙子（smart young feller），将来必成大器。说来奇怪，这评语往往出笼于一场谈话之后——整场谈话里，他除了在恰当的地方应一声「是」，或者「不」，或者「也许您说得对，先生」，不曾贡献过一个字。

===Original===
Selina thought constantly of Dirk’s future. A thousand other thoughts might be racing through her mind during the day﻿—plans for the farm, for the house﻿—but always, over and above and through all these, like the steady beat of a drum penetrating sharper and more urgent sounds﻿—was the thought of Dirk. He did well enough at high school. Not a brilliant student, nor even a very good one. But good enough. Average. And well liked.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜无时不在思虑德克的前程。白天，成百上千种别的念头也许正在她脑子里飞驰——农场的种种盘算、房屋的种种打算——可是凌驾其上、又贯穿其中的，永远是对德克的思虑，像鼓点一般沉稳，穿透那些更尖锐、更急促的声响。他在中学念得够好了。不是出类拔萃的学生，甚至连优等生也算不上。但够好了。平平常常。人缘也好。

===Original===
It was during those careless years of Dirk’s boyhood between nine and fifteen that Selina changed the DeJong acres from a worn-out and down-at-heel truck farm whose scant products brought a second-rate price in a second-rate market to a prosperous and blooming vegetable garden whose output was sought a year in advance by the South Water Street commission merchants. DeJong asparagus with firm white thick stalk bases tapering to a rich green streaked with lavender at the tips. DeJong hothouse tomatoes in February, plump, scarlet, juicy. You paid for a pound a sum Pervus had been glad to get for a bushel.

===Chinese===
正是在德克九岁到十五岁那段无忧无虑的少年岁月里，塞莉娜把德容家的田产从一片凋敝破落的菜园子——出产寥寥，在二流市场卖二流价钱——改造成一座兴旺繁茂的园圃，出产的东西南水街的代卖商提前一年便来包订。德容家的芦笋，笋基粗壮坚实、莹白，往上渐收成浓绿，笋尖上晕出一线淡紫。德容家的暖房番茄，二月里便有了，饱满，绯红，多汁。买一磅的价钱，抵得上当年珀维斯卖一蒲式耳还心满意足的进项。

===Original===
These six or seven years of relentless labour had been no showy success with Selina posing grandly as the New Woman in Business. No, it had been a painful, grubbing, heartbreaking process as is any project that depends on the actual soil for its realization. She drove herself pitilessly. She literally tore a living out of the earth with her two bare hands. Yet there was nothing pitiable about this small energetic woman of thirty-five or forty with her fine soft dark eyes, her clean-cut jaw-line, her shabby decent clothes that were so likely to be spattered with the mud of the road or fields, her exquisite nose with the funny little wrinkle across the bridge when she laughed. Rather, there was something splendid about her; something rich, prophetic. It was the splendour and richness that achievement imparts.

===Chinese===
这六七年毫不容情的劳作，并不是什么风光的成功，塞莉娜也并没有堂而皇之地以「商界新女性」自居。不，那是一段痛苦的、刨土式的、令人心碎的过程——凡是仰仗实实在在的土地来实现的事业，无不如此。她毫不留情地驱策自己。她真是用那两只赤手空拳，从泥土里生生撕扯出一份生计。然而这个三十五六岁、精力充沛的小个子女人身上，没有一点可怜相：一双温柔的黑眼睛，一条干净利落的下颌线，一身寒酸却体面的衣裳，多半溅着路上或田里的泥点，一只精致的鼻子，笑起来鼻梁上会漾起一道有趣的小皱纹。她身上毋宁有某种辉煌的东西；某种丰盈的、先知般的东西。那是成就赋予人的光华与丰采。

===Original===
It is doubtful that she ever could have succeeded without the money borrowed from August Hempel; without his shrewd counsel. She told him this, sometimes. He denied it. “Easier, yes. But you would have found a way, Selina. Some way. Julie, no. But you, yes. You are like that. Me, too. Say, plenty fellers that was butchers with me twenty years ago over on North Clark Street are butchers yet, cutting off a steak or a chop. ‘Good morning, Mrs. Kruger. What’ll it be today?’ ”

===Chinese===
若不是从奥古斯特·亨佩尔那里借来的钱，没有他精明的指点，她究竟成不成得了，很难说。这话她有时对他说。他不认账。「是容易些，这不假。可你总会闯出一条路来，塞莉娜。总归闯得出。朱莉，不行。可你，行。你这人就是这样。我也一样。嗐，二十年前在北克拉克街跟我一块儿当屠夫的那些伙计，如今好些还在案板前呢，切牛排，剁排骨。『早上好，克鲁格太太。今天来点什么？』」

===Original===
The Hempel Packing Company was a vast monster now stretching great arms into Europe, into South America. In some of the yellow journals that had cropped up in the last few years you even saw old Aug himself portrayed in cartoons as an octopus with cold slimy eyes and a hundred writhing reaching tentacles. These bothered Aug a little, though he pretended to laugh at them. “What do they want to go to work and make me out like that for? I sell good meat for all I can get for it. That’s business, ain’t it?”

===Chinese===
如今亨佩尔肉品加工公司已是一头巨怪，长长的臂膀一直伸进欧洲，伸进南美。近几年里冒出来的几家黄色报刊上，你甚至能看到老奥格本人被画进漫画：一只章鱼，眼睛阴冷黏滑，百条触腕扭曲着四下攫取。这些画让奥格有点儿不受用，尽管他装作一笑置之。「他们干嘛要下这份功夫，把我画成那副德行？我卖好肉，能卖多少卖多少。这叫做生意，不是吗？」

===Original===
Dirk had his tasks on the farm. Selina saw to that. But they were not heavy. He left for school at eight in the morning, driving, for the distance was too great for walking. Often it was dark on his return in the late afternoon. Between these hours Selina had accomplished the work of two men. She had two field-helpers on the place now during the busy season and a woman in the house, the wife of Adam Bras, one of the labourers. Jan Snip, too, still worked about the place in the barn, the sheds, tending the coldframes and hothouses, doing odd jobs of carpentering. He distrusted Selina’s newfangled methods, glowered at any modern piece of machinery, predicted dire things when Selina bought the twenty acres that comprised the old Bouts place adjoining the DeJong farm.

===Chinese===
农场上的活计，德克有他的一份。这一点塞莉娜把得紧。但都不重。他早上八点赶车上学，路太远，走不动。傍晚回来时天往往已经黑了。在这中间的时辰里，塞莉娜干完了两个男人的活。如今农忙时节田里雇着两个帮工，屋里雇着一个女工，是雇工亚当·布拉斯（Adam Bras）的妻子。扬·斯尼普也还在场上做事，谷仓啊，棚屋啊，照管冷床和暖房，打点木工零活。他信不过塞莉娜那套新奇法子，见了任何一件新式机器都要横眉竖眼；等到塞莉娜买下德容农场紧邻的那片旧鲍茨家庄地——足有二十英亩——他更是预言大祸临头。

===Original===
“You bit off more as you can chaw,” he told her. “You choke yet. You see.”

===Chinese===
「你咬下的口，比你能嚼得动的多，」他对她说。「早晚噎着你。你瞧着吧。」

===Original===
By the time Dirk returned from school the rough work of the day was over. His food was always hot, appetizing, plentiful. The house was neat, comfortable. Selina had installed a bathroom﻿—one of the two bathrooms in High Prairie. The neighbourhood was still rocking with the shock of this when it was informed by Jan that Selina and Dirk ate with candles lighted on the supper table. High Prairie slapped its thigh and howled with mirth.

===Chinese===
等德克放学回来，一天里粗重的活计都已收场。他的饭食总是热的，喷香，管够。屋子整洁，舒适。塞莉娜装了一间浴室——全高草原仅有的两间浴室之一。左邻右舍还被这桩事震得东倒西歪，扬又来告诉他们：塞莉娜和德克吃晚饭时桌上还点蜡烛。高草原拍着大腿，笑得前仰后合。

===Original===
“Cabbages is beautiful,” said old Klaas Pool when he heard this. “Cabbages is beautiful I betcha.”

===Chinese===
「卷心菜可真是美啊，」老克拉斯·普尔听到这事时说。「卷心菜就是美，俺敢打赌。」

===Original===
Selina, during the years of the boy’s adolescence, had never urged him to a decision about his future. That, she decided, would come. As the farm prospered and the pressure of necessity lifted she tried, in various ingenious ways, to extract from him some unconscious sign of definite preference for this calling, that profession. As in her leanest days she had bought an occasional book at the cost of much-needed shoes for herself so now she bought many of them with money that another woman would have used for luxury or adornments. Years of personal privation had not killed her love of fine soft silken things, mellow colouring, exquisite workmanship. But they had made it impossible for her to covet these things for herself. She loved to see them, to feel them. Could not wear them. Years later, when she could well afford a French hat in one of the Michigan Avenue millinery shops, she would look at the silk and satin trifles blooming in the windows like gay brilliant flowers in a conservatory﻿—and would buy an untrimmed “shape” for $2.95 in Field’s basement. The habit of a lifetime is strong. Just once she made herself buy one of these costly silk-and-feather extravagances, going about the purchase deliberately and coldly as a man gets drunk once for the experience. The hat had cost twenty-two dollars. She never had worn it.

===Chinese===
儿子青春期这几年里，塞莉娜从不去催他拿定关于前程的主意。那个，她断定，自会水到渠成。随着农场兴旺、生计的压迫渐松，她想出种种巧法子，想从他身上引出些不自觉的迹象，看他对这一行、那一业可有什么确定的偏好。当年手头最紧的日子，她能拿自己急需的鞋子换一本偶尔的书；如今她大批地买书，花的是别的女人会拿去买奢侈品、置首饰的钱。多年的自我匮乏，没有磨灭她对精美柔软的丝质之物、温润的色彩、精妙的做工的喜爱。却叫她再不可能为自己贪求这些东西了。她爱看它们，爱摸它们。穿不得。多年以后，当她完全买得起密歇根大道上任何一家女帽店的法国帽子时，她会望着橱窗里盛开的那些绸缎小玩意儿，像暖房里明艳娇媚的花朵——然后转身去菲尔德百货（Field's）的地下层，花 2.95 美元买一顶素「帽坯」。一生的习惯是顽强的。只有一回，她强迫自己买了那些昂贵的绸缎羽毛铺张货里的一顶，下手时刻意而冷淡，像一个男人为了体验而特意大醉一场。那顶帽子花了二十二块钱。她从来没能戴过它。

===Original===
Until Dirk was sixteen she had been content to let him develop as naturally as possible, and to absorb impressions unconsciously from the traps she so guilefully left about him. Books on the lives of great men﻿—lives of Lincoln, of Washington, Gladstone, Disraeli, Voltaire. History. Books on painting, charmingly illustrated. Books on architecture; law; medicine, even. She subscribed to two of the best engineering magazines. There was a shed which he was free to use as a workshop, fitted up with all sorts of tools. He did not use it much, after the first few weeks. He was pleasantly and mildly interested in all these things; held by none of them. Selina had thought of Roelf when they were fitting up the workshop. The Pools had heard from Roelf just once since his flight from the farm. A letter had come from France. In it was a sum of money for Geertje and Jozina﻿—a small sum to take the trouble to send all the way from an outlandish country, the well-to-do Pool household thought. Geertje was married now to Vander Sijde’s son Gerrit and living on a farm out Low Prairie way. Jozina had a crazy idea that she wanted to go into the city as a nurse. Roelf’s small gift of money made little difference in their day. They never knew the struggle that the impecunious young Paris art student had had to save it sou by sou. Selina had never heard from him. But one day years later she had come running to Dirk with an illustrated magazine in her hand.

===Chinese===
在德克满十六岁之前，她一直甘心让他尽可能自然地长大，不知不觉地吸进那些她处心积虑布在他四下的「陷阱」留给他的印象。伟人生平的书——林肯的、华盛顿的、格莱斯顿的、迪斯累利的、伏尔泰的生平。历史。讲绘画的书，插图印得迷人。讲建筑的书；法律的书；甚至还有医学。她订了两份最好的工程杂志。有一间棚屋随他用作工场，各样家伙什置备齐全。过了最初几个星期，他就不怎么用了。这些东西他样样都觉着有趣、喜欢，却无一能拴住他。布置那间工场时，塞莉娜想起了鲁尔夫。自打鲁尔夫从农场出走，普尔家只得到过他一回音信。法国来了一封信。里面是给海尔切和约西娜的一笔钱——特意从那么个天边外的国度寄这么一小笔来，殷实的普尔家觉得犯不上。海尔切如今嫁了范德赛德家的儿子格里特，住在低草原那边的一座农场上。约西娜起了个疯念头，想进城当护士。鲁尔夫寄来的那点钱，于他们的日子没添什么光景。他们从不知道，那个手头拮据的巴黎学艺青年，是何等一个苏（sou，法国小铜板）一个苏地省下，才攒出这笔钱来。塞莉娜从没得到过他的音信。可是多年后的一天，她手里攥着一本画报，一路跑去找德克。

===Original===
“Look!” she had cried, and pointed to a picture. He had rarely seen her so excited, so stirred. The illustration showed a photographic reproduction of a piece of sculpture﻿—a woman’s figure. It was called The Seine. A figure sinuous, snakelike, graceful, revolting, beautiful, terrible. The face alluring, insatiable, generous, treacherous, all at once. It was the Seine that fed the fertile valley land; the Seine that claimed a thousand bloated lifeless floating Things; the red-eyed hag of 1793; the dimpling coquette of 1650. Beneath the illustration a line or two﻿—Roelf Pool﻿ ﻿… Salon﻿ ﻿… American﻿ ﻿… future﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
「看！」她当时喊道，手指着一张画。他难得见她这样激动，这样心潮翻涌。那幅插图是一件雕塑作品的照相翻印——一个女人的形体。题名《塞纳河》（The Seine）。一个蜿蜒的、蛇一般的形体，优雅，可憎，美丽，可怖。那张脸妩媚、贪得无厌、慷慨、阴险，一时并具。这是滋养丰饶谷地的塞纳河；是收纳千百具浮肿的、没了气息的漂泊之物的塞纳河；是 1793 年红眼珠的老巫婆；是 1650 年笑出梨涡的风骚女。插图底下短短一两行——鲁尔夫·普尔……沙龙（Salon）……美国人……前程……

===Original===
“It’s Roelf!” Selina had cried. “Roelf. Little Roelf Pool!” Tears in her eyes. Dirk had been politely interested. But then he had never known him, really. He had heard his mother speak of him, but﻿—

===Chinese===
「是鲁尔夫！」塞莉娜当时喊道。「鲁尔夫。小鲁尔夫·普尔！」眼里噙着泪。德克客客气气地表示了兴趣。可他到底没有真正认识过他。他听母亲说起过他，不过——

===Original===
Selina showed the picture to the Pools, driving over there one evening to surprise them with it. Mrs. Klaas Pool had been horrified at the picture of a nude woman’s figure; had cried “*Og heden!*” in disgust, and had seemed to think that Selina had brought it over in a spirit of spite. Was she going to show it to the rest of High Prairie!

===Chinese===
一天傍晚，塞莉娜赶车到普尔家去，要拿这张画给他们个惊喜。克拉斯·普尔太太见了那个裸女形体的画，骇得不轻；厌恶地叫了一声「*Og heden!*（哎呀天哪）」，而且倒像是认定塞莉娜是存心使坏，才把这东西送上门来的。她莫非还打算拿给高草原其余的人瞧不成！

===Original===
Selina understood High Prairie folk better now, though not altogether, even after almost twenty years of living amongst them. A cold people, yet kindly. Suspicious, yet generous. Distrustful of all change, yet progressing by sheer force of thrift and unceasing labour. Unimaginative for generations, only to produce﻿—a Roelf Pool.

===Chinese===
如今塞莉娜对高草原人的了解深了些，虽说不算透彻——即便同他们一起生活了将近二十年。一个冷淡的民族，却心地和善。多疑，却慷慨。对一切变更都不信任，却凭着节俭和不辍劳作的蛮力步步向前。世代缺少想象，偏偏生出——一个鲁尔夫·普尔来。

===Original===
She tried now to explain the meaning of the figure Roelf had moulded so masterfully. “You see, it’s supposed to represent the Seine. The River Seine that flows through Paris into the countryside beyond. The whole history of Paris﻿—of France﻿—is bound up in the Seine; intertwined with it. Terrible things, and magnificent things. It flows just beneath the Louvre. You can see it from the Bastille. On its largest island stands Notre Dame. The Seine has seen such things, Mrs. Pool!﻿—”

===Chinese===
这会儿她试着讲讲鲁尔夫塑造得那样大师手笔的形体，讲讲它的意思。「你看，它是要表现塞纳河。那条流过巴黎、再流进远处乡野的塞纳河。巴黎的全部历史——法兰西的全部历史——都系于塞纳河一身；同它交织在一处。可怕的事，还有壮丽的事。它就从卢浮宫底下流过。从巴士底狱望得见它。它最大的岛上立着巴黎圣母院。塞纳河见识过多少世事啊，普尔太太！——」

===Original===
“What *dom* talk!” interrupted the late widow. “A river can’t see. Anybody knows that.”

===Chinese===
「这是什么*dom*（荷兰语：傻）话！」新近守寡的普尔老太太打断她。「河又不会看。谁不知道这个。」

===Original===
At seventeen Dirk and Selina talked of the year to come. He was going to a university. But to what university? And what did he want to study? We-e-ll, hard to say. Kind of a general course, wasn’t there? Some languages﻿—little French or something﻿—and political economy, and some literature and maybe history.

===Chinese===
德克十七岁上，他和塞莉娜谈起将来的一年。他要上大学了。可是上哪一所大学呢？又想学什么？呃——这个么，难说。不是有那种笼统的综合课程吗？学几种语言——一点法文什么的——加上政治经济学，再加点文学，兴许还有历史。

===Original===
“Oh,” Selina had said. “Yes. General. Of course, if a person wanted to be an architect, why, I suppose Cornell would be the place. Or Harvard for law. Or Boston Tech for engineering, or﻿—”

===Chinese===
「哦，」塞莉娜说过。「是啊。综合的。当然啦，人要是想当建筑师，那，我想康奈尔（Cornell）就是该去的地方。学法律呢，就是哈佛。学工程呢，就是波士顿理工（Boston Tech），要不——」

===Original===
Oh, yeh, if a fellow wanted any of those things. Good idea, though, to take a kind of general course until you found out exactly what you wanted to do. Languages and literature and that kind of thing.

===Chinese===
哦，是啊，人要是当真想要那些东西的话。不过，先念个笼统的综合课程，等弄明白了自己到底要干什么再说，倒是个好主意。语言啦文学啦那一类的东西。

===Original===
Selina was rather delighted than otherwise. That, she knew, was the way they did it in England. You sent your son to a university not to cram some technical course into him, or to railroad him through a book-knowledge of some profession. You sent him so that he might develop in an atmosphere of books, of learning; spending relaxed hours in the companionship of men who taught for the love of teaching; whose informal talks before a study fire were more richly valuable than whole courses of classroom lectures. She had read of these things in English novels. Oxford. Cambridge. Dons. Ivy. Punting. Prints. Mullioned windows. Books. Discussion. Literary clubs.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜与其说意外，不如说正中下怀。她知道，英国人就是这么办的。你送儿子上大学，不是往他脑袋里硬灌某门技术课程，也不是押着他囫囵吞下一门专业的书本知识。你送他去，是要让他在书籍与学问的空气里长养；从从容容地消磨时光，陪伴他的是一群为爱教书而教书的人，他们在书房炉火前信口谈出的东西，比一整套一整套的课堂讲义还金贵。这些她在英国小说里读到过。牛津。剑桥。学院的导师（dons）。常春藤。撑篙。版画。直棂窗。书籍。谈论。文学社。

===Original===
This was England. An older civilization, of course. But there must be something of that in American universities. And if that was what Dirk wanted she was glad. Glad! A reaching after true beauty.

===Chinese===
这是英格兰。当然是更古老的文明。可美国的大学里，想必也有几分这样的东西。倘若德克想要的就是这个，那她高兴。高兴啊！那是对真美的一番追逐。

===Original===
You heard such wonderful things about Midwest University, in Chicago. On the south side. It was new, yes. But those Gothic buildings gave an effect, somehow, of age and permanence (the smoke and cinders from the Illinois Central suburban trains were largely responsible for that, as well as the soft coal from a thousand neighbouring chimneys). And there actually was ivy. Undeniable ivy, and mullioned windows.

===Chinese===
关于芝加哥的中西部大学（Midwest University），你听到的净是了不得的说法。在城南。它确实年轻。可那一座座哥特式楼群，不知怎么，自有一股古老而永固的气象（伊利诺伊中央铁路郊区列车喷出的烟和煤屑对此功劳不小，周围千家万户烟囱里的烟煤也一样）。而且当真有常春藤。不容抵赖的常春藤，还有直棂窗。

===Original===
Dirk had suggested it, not she. The entrance requirements were quite mild. Harvard? Yale? Oh, those fellows all had wads of money. Eugene Arnold had his own car at New Haven.

===Chinese===
这主意是德克提的，不是她。那学校的入学要求松得很。哈佛？耶鲁？哦，上那些地方的家伙们个个都有大把的钱。尤金·阿诺德在纽黑文（New Haven）上学，开的是自己的汽车。

===Original===
In that case, they decided, Midwest University, in Chicago, on the south side near the lake, would do splendidly. For a general course, sort of. The world lay ahead of Dirk. It was like the childhood game of counting buttons.

===Chinese===
那么，他们便商定：芝加哥的中西部大学，在城南、湖边，再好不过。念个综合课程，就算是吧。世界铺展在德克面前。就像小时候数纽扣的占卜游戏。

===Original===
Rich man, poor man, beggar-man, thief,

					Doctor, lawyer, merchant, chief.

===Chinese===
阔佬，穷汉，叫花子，小偷，

					医生，律师，买卖人，首领。

===Original===
Together they counted Dirk’s mental buttons but it never came out twice the same. It depended on the suit you happened to be wearing, of course. Eugene Arnold was going to take law at Yale. He said it would be necessary if he was going into the business. He didn’t put it just that way, when talking to Dirk. He said the damned old hog business. Pauline (she insisted that they call her Paula now) was at a girls’ school up the Hudson﻿—one of those schools that never advertise even in the front of the thirty-five-cent magazines.

===Chinese===
娘儿俩一起数着德克心里的纽扣，可数来数去，没有两回结果相同。这自然要看你身上碰巧穿着哪件衣裳。尤金·阿诺德要去耶鲁读法律。他说既然要进那一行，这就免不了。跟德克说起时，他可不是这么讲的。他说的是那该死的老猪生意。波琳（她如今非要大家管她叫宝拉（Paula）不可）在哈德逊河上游的一所女子学校念书——那种连三毛五分钱一本的杂志前页广告都不肯登的学校。

===Original===
So, at eighteen, it had been Midwest University for Dirk. It was a much more economical plan than would have resulted from the choice of an eastern college. High Prairie heard that Dirk DeJong was going away to college. A neighbour’s son said, “Going to Wisconsin? Agricultural course there?”

===Chinese===
就这样，十八岁上，德克进的是中西部大学。这比上东部的大学省出一大笔。高草原听说德克·德容要出门上大学了。一户邻居的儿子问：「去威斯康星？上那儿的农科？」

===Original===
“My gosh, no!” Dirk had answered. He told this to Selina, laughing. But she had not laughed.

===Chinese===
「我的老天，才不呢！」德克当时答道。他把这话学给塞莉娜听，还笑着。她可没笑。

===Original===
“I’d like to take that course myself, if you must know. They say it’s wonderful.” She looked at him, suddenly. “Dirk, you wouldn’t like to take it, would you? To go to Madison, I mean. Is that what you’d like?”

===Chinese===
「你要是非知道不可——那门课我自己倒想去念呢。听说好极了。」她忽然看着他。「德克，你不会是想上那个吧？我是说，去麦迪逊。你想的就是那个吗？」

===Original===
He stared. “Me! No!﻿ ﻿… Unless you want me to, Mother. Then I would, gladly. I hate your working like this, on the farm, while I go off to school. It makes me feel kind of rotten, having my mother working for me. The other fellows﻿—”

===Chinese===
他瞪大了眼。「我！不！……除非你要我去，妈妈。那样我乐意去。我见不得你这样在农场上苦干，自己倒跑出去上学。母亲为我做工，这叫我心里怪不是滋味的。别的同学人家——」

===Original===
“I’m doing the work I’m interested in, for the person I love best in the world. I’d be lost﻿—unhappy﻿—without the farm. If the city creeps up on me here, as they predict it will, I don’t know what I shall do.”

===Chinese===
「我做的是我有兴味的工作，为的是这世上我最爱的人。离了农场，我会六神无主——会不快活的。要是城市真像他们预言的那样朝我这边爬过来，我不知道自己该怎么办。」

===Original===
But Dirk had a prediction of his own to make. “Chicago’ll never grow this way, with all those steel mills and hunkies to the south of us. The north side is going to be the place to live. It is already.”

===Chinese===
可德克也有他自己的预言。「芝加哥绝不会朝这边发展，咱们南边净是钢厂和那些东欧佬（hunkies，对东欧移民的蔑称）。城北才是往后住人的地方。现在已经是了。」

===Original===
“The place for whom?”

===Chinese===
「什么人住的地方？」

===Original===
“For the people with money.”

===Chinese===
「有钱人住的地方。」

===Original===
She smiled then so that you saw the funny little wrinkle across her nose. “Well, then the south section of Chicago is going to be all right for us yet a while.”

===Chinese===
她于是笑了，笑得你能看见她鼻梁上那道有趣的小皱纹。「好啊，那芝加哥城南这一片，暂时倒还配咱们住一阵子。」

===Original===
“Just you wait till I’m successful. Then there’ll be no more working for you.”

===Chinese===
「你只管等我成功。到那时，你就再不用干活了。」

===Original===
“What do you mean by ‘successful,’ Sobig?” She had not called him that in years. But now the old nickname came to her tongue perhaps because they were speaking of his future, his success. “What do you mean by ‘successful,’ Sobig?”

===Chinese===
「你说的『成功』是什么意思，『这么大』？」她已有多年没这么叫过他了。可这会儿，这个旧诨名又上了她的舌尖，兴许正是因为他们谈的是他的前程、他的成功。「你说的『成功』是什么意思，『这么大』？」

===Original===
“Rich. Lots of money.”

===Chinese===
「有钱。很多很多钱。」

===Original===
“Oh, no, Dirk! No! That’s not success. Roelf﻿—the thing Roelf does﻿—that’s success.”

===Chinese===
「哦，不，德克！不！那不是成功。鲁尔夫——鲁尔夫做的那种事——那才是成功。」

===Original===
“Oh, well, if you have money enough you can buy the things he makes, and have ’em. That’s almost as good, isn’t it?”

===Chinese===
「哦，这个么，钱要是够了，他做出来的东西你尽管买来，据为己有。这也就差不多一样好了，不是吗？」

===Original===
Midwest University had sprung up almost literally overnight on the property that had been the site of the Midway Plaisance during the World’s Fair in Chicago in ’93. One man’s millions had been the magic wand that, waved over a bare stretch of prairie land, had produced a seat of learning. The university guide book spoke of him reverently as the Founder, capitalizing the word as one does the Deity. The student body spoke of him with somewhat less veneration. They called him Coal-Oil Johnny. He had already given thirty millions to the university and still the insatiable maw of this institute of learning yawned for more. When oil went up a fraction of a cent they said, “Guess Coal-Oil Johnny’s fixing to feed us another million.”

===Chinese===
中西部大学几乎是当真一夜之间冒出来的，占了 1893 年芝加哥世界博览会中途林荫道（Midway Plaisance）的旧址。一个人的百万家财成了那根魔杖，在一片光秃秃的草原上空一挥，就变出一座学府。大学指南里毕恭毕敬地称他为「创办人」（the Founder），字头大写，同称呼神明一般。学生们提起他，敬意可就没那么足了。他们管他叫「煤油约翰尼」（Coal-Oil Johnny）。他已经捐给大学三千万，这所学府那张填不满的大口还张着要。油价每涨一分一厘，他们就说：「看样子煤油约翰尼又要喂咱们一百万了。」

===Original===
Dirk commenced his studies at Midwest University in the autumn of 1909. His first year was none too agreeable, as is usually the case in first years. He got on well, though. A large proportion of the men students were taking law, which accounts for the great number of real-estate salesmen and insurance agents now doing business in and about Chicago. Before the end of the first semester he was popular. He was a natural-born floor committeeman and badges bloomed in his buttonhole. Merely by donning a ready-made dress suit he could give it a made-to-order air. He had great natural charm of manner. The men liked him, and the girls, too. He learned to say, “Got Pol Econ at ten,” which meant that he took Political Economy at that hour; and “I’d like to cut Psyk,” meant that he was not up on his approaching lesson in Applied Psychology. He rarely “cut” a class. He would have felt that this was unfair and disloyal to his mother. Some of his fellow students joked about this faithfulness to his classes. “Person would think you were an Unclassified,” they said.

===Chinese===
1909 年秋，德克在中西部大学开课了。头一年总不大痛快，这是常例，他也不例外。不过他混得不坏。男生里好大一拨在念法律——这正说明了如今在芝加哥城里城外做买卖的地产推销员和保险经纪人何以如此之多。第一学期还没到期末，他就已经吃得开了。他天生是块当舞会干事的料，纽扣眼里徽章开得像花。一套现成的夜礼服上了身，他也能穿出定做的气派。他天生一副好风度。男的喜欢他，女的也喜欢他。他学会了说「十点钟有政经（Pol Econ）」，意思是他那个钟点上政治经济学课；还说「真想逃心理（Psyk）」，意思是他那门应用心理学的功课还没温熟。他难得「逃」一堂课。逃课会叫他觉得既亏心又对不起母亲。同学里有人拿他这份守本分打趣。「人家还当你是非注册生（Unclassified）呢，」他们说。

===Original===
The Unclassifieds were made up, for the most part, of earnest and rather middle-aged students whose education was a delayed blooming. They usually were not enrolled for a full course, or were taking double work feverishly. The Classifieds, on the other hand, were the regularly enrolled students, pretty well of an age (between seventeen and twenty-three) who took their education with a sprinkling of sugar. Of the Unclassified students the University catalogue said:

===Chinese===
非注册生们大抵是一班认真的、上了点年纪的学生，他们的教育是一场迟开的花。他们通常不注册修满全部课程，就是发着狠一气修双份的功课。注册生（Classifieds）则相反，是正式注册在籍的学生，年纪齐整（十七到二十三岁之间），受起教育来还要撒上一层糖。关于非注册生，大学章程上写道：

===Original===
Persons at least twenty-one years of age, not seeking a degree, may be admitted through the office of the University Examiner to the courses of instruction offered in the University, as unclassified students. They shall present evidence of successful experience as a teacher or *other valuable educative experience in practical life*.﻿ ﻿… They are ineligible for public appearance.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
凡年满二十一岁、不攻学位者，经大学考试官办公室核准，得以非注册生（unclassified students）身份修习本校所设课程。彼等须出示任教有成之资历，或*其他于实际生活中宝贵的教育经历*。……彼等无资格于公开场合露面。……

===Original===
You saw them the Cinderellas and the Smikes of this temple of learning.

===Chinese===
你看得出来，他们是这座学问殿堂里的灰姑娘们和斯迈克们。

===Original===
The Classifieds and the Unclassifieds rarely mixed. Not age alone, but purpose separated them. The Classifieds, boys and girls, were, for the most part, slim young lads with caps and pipes and sweaters, their talk of football, baseball, girls; slim young girls in sheer shirtwaists with pink ribbons run through the corset covers showing beneath, pleated skirts that switched delightfully as they strolled across the campus arm in arm, their talk of football games, fudge, clothes, boys. They cut classes whenever possible. The Student Body. Midwest turned them out by the hundreds﻿—almost by the link, one might say, as Aug Hempel’s sausage factory turned out its fine plump sausages, each one exactly like the one behind and the one ahead of it. So many hundreds graduated in this year’s class. So many more hundreds to be graduated in next year’s class. Occasionally an unruly sausage burst its skin and was discarded. They attended a university because their parents﻿—thrifty shopkeepers, manufacturers, merchants, or professional men and their good wives﻿—wanted their children to have an education. Were ambitious for them. “I couldn’t have it myself, and always regretted it. Now I want my boy (or girl) to have a good education that’ll fit ’em for the battle of life. This is an age of specialization, let me tell you.”

===Chinese===
注册生和非注册生难得混在一处。隔开他们的不只是年纪，还有来意。注册生们，男孩女孩，大抵是头戴便帽、嘴叼烟斗、身穿毛衣的精瘦少年，谈的是橄榄球、棒球、姑娘；是穿着薄纱女衬衫、束胸衣上透出粉红缎带、百褶裙在挽着胳膊漫步穿过校园时快活摆动的精瘦少女，谈的是球赛、软糖、衣裳、小伙子。能逃的课他们一概逃。学生大众。中西部大学成百成百地出产他们——简直可以说是一节一节地出，就像奥格·亨佩尔的香肠厂出产它那些肥美体面的香肠，每一根都同它前头那根、后头那根一模一样。今年这一届毕业这么几百人。明年那一届还要毕业那么几百人。偶尔有一根不守本分的香肠胀破了肠衣，就给扔掉了。他们来上大学，是因为他们的父母——勤俭的店主、厂主、商人或自由职业者，连同他们的贤内助——要儿女受个教育。为他们怀着雄心。「我自己当年没这个福分，一直引为憾事。如今我要我的儿子（或者女儿）受个好教育，好叫他们打得赢人生这一仗。如今是个讲专门化的时代，我告诉你。」

===Original===
Football, fudge, I-said-to-Jim, I-said-to-Bessie.

===Chinese===
橄榄球，软糖，我跟吉姆说，我跟贝茜说。

===Original===
The Unclassifieds would no more have deliberately cut a class than they would have thrown their sparse weekly budget-allowance into the gutter. If it had been physically possible they would have attended two classes at once, listened to two lectures, prepared two papers simultaneously. Drab and earnest women between thirty and forty-eight, their hair not an ornament, but something to be pinned up quickly out of the way, their clothes a covering, their shoes not even smartly “sensible,” but just shoes, scuffed, patched, utilitarian. The men were serious, shabby, often spectacled; dandruff on their coat collars; their lined, anxious faces in curious contrast to the fresh, boyish, carefree countenances of the Classifieds. They said, carefully, almost sonorously, “Political Economy. Applied Psychology.” Most of them had worked ten years, fifteen years for this deferred schooling. This one had had to support a mother; that one a family of younger brothers and sisters. This plump woman of thirty-nine, with the jolly kindly face, had had a paralyzed father. Another had known merely poverty, grinding, sordid poverty, with fifteen years of painful penny savings to bring true this gloriously realized dream of a university education. Here was one studying to be a trained Social Service Worker. She had done everything from housework as a servant girl to clerking in a 5- and 10-cent store. She had studied evenings; saved pennies, nickels, dimes, quarters. *Other valuable educative experience in practical life.* They had had it, God knows.

===Chinese===
非注册生们断不会存心逃一堂课，正如他们断不会把每周那点可怜的津贴扔进阴沟。倘若体力上办得到，他们会同时上两堂课，同时听两场讲演，同时赶两份论文。三十到四十八岁之间、颜色灰暗而心气认真的女人们，头发不是妆饰，是赶紧用卡子别起来图省事的东西；衣裳是遮体的；鞋子连体面的「实惠」都算不上，就只是鞋——磨旧的、打过补丁的、单讲实用的。男人们严肃，寒酸，多半戴眼镜；外套领上落着头屑；一张张刻着皱纹的焦虑的脸，同注册生们那些鲜嫩、孩子气、无忧无虑的面孔两相对照，煞是古怪。他们字斟句酌、近乎朗声地说：「政治经济学。应用心理学。」他们大多数人为这场迟来的学业苦干了十年、十五年。这一位得养活母亲；那一位得拉扯一群弟弟妹妹。这位三十九岁、胖乎乎、一脸和气的女人，瘫了个父亲。另一位尝过的仅仅是穷，磨人的、腌臜的穷，一分一分辛辛苦苦攒了十五年，才换来这个光辉实现的大学教育的梦。这里还有一位在修课，要当一名受过训练的社会服务工作者。她什么都干过，从当女仆做家务，到五分一角（5-and-10-cent）小杂货店里站柜台。她夜里读书；攒下一枚枚一分、五分、一角、两毛五。*其他于实际生活中宝贵的教育经历。*老天知道，他们全都有过。

===Original===
They regarded the university at first with the love-blind eyes of a bridegroom who looks with the passionate tenderness of possession upon his mistress for whom he has worked and waited through the years of his youth. The university was to bring back that vanished youth﻿—and something more. Wisdom. Knowledge. Power. Understanding. They would have died for it﻿—they almost had, what with privation, self-denial, work.

===Chinese===
起初，他们望着这所大学，用的是新郎官那双被爱蒙住的眼睛——他望着自己拿整个青春的岁月苦干苦候、如今终归占有的心上人，怀着占有的炽烈柔情。大学将还给他们那逝去的青春——还不止于此。智慧。学识。力量。悟性。他们肯为它死——也几乎真死过了，靠的是匮乏、克己、劳作。

===Original===
They came with love clasped close in their two hands, an offertory. “Take me!” they cried. “I come with all I have. Devotion, hope, desire to learn, a promise to be a credit to you. I have had experience, bittersweet experience. I have known the battle. See, here are my scars. I can bring to your classrooms much that is valuable. I ask only for bread﻿—the bread of knowledge.”

===Chinese===
他们双手紧紧捧着爱而来，像捧着一份祭献。「收下我！」他们喊道。「我带着我的一切来。热忱，希望，求知的渴望，还有为你增光的许诺。我阅历过，苦甜参半的阅历。我打过那场仗。看，这是我的伤疤。我能带进你课堂的东西，许多都极可宝贵。我只求面包——知识的粮食。」

===Original===
And the University gave them a stone.

===Chinese===
而大学给了他们一块石头。

===Original===
“Get on to the hat!” said the Classifieds, humorously, crossing the campus. “A fright!”

===Chinese===
「快瞧那顶帽子！」注册生们穿过校园时打趣道。「丑八怪！」

===Original===
The professors found them a shade too eager, perhaps; too inquiring; demanding too much. They stayed after class and asked innumerable questions. They bristled with interrogation. They were prone to hold forth in the classroom, “Well, I have found it to be the case in my experience that﻿—”

===Chinese===
教授们觉得他们兴许急切得过了头；太好问；索取得太多。下了课他们不走，问出数不清的问题。他们浑身竖满问号。课堂上他们动不动就要开讲：「嗐，依我个人的经验，我发现——」

===Original===
But the professor preferred to do the lecturing himself. If there was to be any experience related it should come from the teacher’s platform, not the student’s chair. Besides, this sort of thing interfered with the routine; kept you from covering ground fast enough. The period bell rang, and there you were, halfway through the day’s prescribed lesson.

===Chinese===
可是教授宁愿自己来讲。要谈阅历，也该从讲台上谈出来，不该从学生的座椅上。何况这种事打乱教学常轨；拖得你不能按进度把课往前赶。课时铃一响，你才讲到当天规定课程的一半。

===Original===
In his first year Dirk made the almost fatal mistake of being rather friendly with one of these Unclassifieds﻿—a female Unclassified. She was in his Pol Econ class and sat next to him. A large, good-humoured, plump girl, about thirty-eight, with a shiny skin which she never powdered and thick hair that exuded a disagreeable odour of oil. She was sympathetic and jolly, but her clothes were a fright, the Classifieds would have told you, and no matter how cold the day there was always a half-moon of stain showing under her armpits. She had a really fine mind, quick, eager, balanced, almost judicial. She knew just which references were valuable, which useless. Just how to go about getting information for next day’s class; for the weekly paper to be prepared. Her name was Schwengauer﻿—Mattie Schwengauer. Terrible!

===Chinese===
一年级时，德克犯了个差点致命的错误：同这些非注册生里的一个颇为要好——一个女非注册生。她同他一起上政经课，坐他旁边。一个高大、和气、胖乎乎的姑娘，三十八岁上下，皮肤油亮，从不扑粉，浓密的头发泛出一股不好闻的头油气。她热心，快活，可她的衣裳吓人，注册生们会这样告诉你，而且无论天多冷，她腋下总有一弯汗渍的月牙。她有一副真正出色的头脑，敏捷，热切，稳健，近乎法官般的公允。她一清二楚哪些参考书有价值，哪些没用。一清二楚第二天的课要用的资料该上哪儿去找、每周的论文该怎么做。她姓施文高尔（Schwengauer）——玛蒂·施文高尔（Mattie Schwengauer）。这名字，真够呛！

===Original===
“Here,” she would say good-naturedly, to Dirk. “You don’t need to read all those. My, no! I’ll tell you. You’ll get exactly what you want by reading pages 256 to 273 in Blaine’s; 549 to 567 in Jaeckel; and the first eleven﻿—no, twelve﻿—pages of Trowbridge’s report. That’ll give you practically everything you need.”

===Chinese===
「喏，」她会好脾气地对德克说。「这些你用不着全读。嗐，不用！我告诉你。布莱恩（Blaine）那本读 256 到 273 页，耶克尔（Jaeckel）那本读 549 到 567 页，再读特罗布里奇（Trowbridge）报告的头十一页——不对，十二页。你要的东西差不多就全有了。」

===Original===
Dirk was grateful. Her notes were always copious, perfect. She never hesitated to let him copy them. They got in the way of walking out of the classroom together, across the campus. She told him something of herself.

===Chinese===
德克很感激她。她的笔记总是记得又全又好。让他抄，她也从不迟疑。两人渐渐惯于一块儿走出教室，穿过校园。她跟他讲了些自己的身世。

===Original===
“Your people farmers!” Surprised, she looked at his well-cut clothes, his slim, strong, unmarked hands, his smart shoes and cap. “Why, so are mine. Iowa.” She pronounced it Ioway. “I lived on the farm all my life till I was twenty-seven. I always wanted to go away to school, but we never had the money and I couldn’t come to town to earn because I was the oldest, and Ma was sickly after Emma﻿—that’s the youngest﻿—there are nine of us﻿—was born. Ma was anxious I should go and Pa was willing, but it couldn’t be. No fault of theirs. One year the summer would be so hot, with no rain hardly from spring till fall, and the corn would just dry up on the stalks, like paper. The next year it would be so wet the seed would rot in the ground. Ma died when I was twenty-six. The kids were all pretty well grown up by that time. Pa married again in a year and I went to Des Moines to work. I stayed there six years but I didn’t save much on account of my brother. He was kind of wild. He had come to Des Moines, too, after Pa married. He and Aggie﻿—that’s the second wife﻿—didn’t get along. I came to Chicago about five years ago.﻿ ﻿… I’ve done all kinds of work, I guess, except digging in a coal mine. I’d have done that if I’d had to.”

===Chinese===
「你家的人是庄稼汉！」她惊讶地打量他那身剪裁合体的衣裳、那双瘦长有力而不见茧痕的手、那双时髦的鞋子和便帽。「嗐，我家也是。艾奥瓦（Iowa）人。」她把它念成了「艾奥威」（Ioway）。「我在农场上一直住到二十七岁。我老想出门去上学，可家里从没有钱，我又不能进城去挣，因为我是老大，而艾玛——那是老幺——我们一共九个——生下以后，妈的身体就一直坏。妈巴不得我去，爸也点头，可就是不成。不怪他们。有的年景，夏天热得邪乎，从开春到入秋几乎滴雨不下，玉米就在秆上干死了，跟纸似的。第二年又涝得种子烂在地里。我二十六岁那年，妈没了。到那时候，弟弟妹妹们差不多都拉扯大了。爸过一年续了弦，我就去得梅因（Des Moines）做工。在那儿待了六年，可没攒下多少，都因为我弟弟。他有点野。爸再婚以后，他也去了得梅因。他跟阿琪（Aggie）——就是后妈——合不来。我到芝加哥大概五年了。……我猜除了下煤窑，什么样的活儿我都干过了。真到那一步，煤窑我也下。」

===Original===
She told him all this ingenuously, simply. Dirk felt drawn toward her, sorry for her. His was a nature quick to sympathy. Something she said now stirred him while it bewildered him a little, too.

===Chinese===
这一切她说得坦坦荡荡、简简单单。德克觉得同她亲近，也替她难过。他生来一副易于动怜的性子。这会儿她说的某句话打动了他，也叫他有点摸不着头脑。

===Original===
“You can’t have any idea what it means to me to be here﻿ ﻿… All those years! I used to dream about it. Even now it seems to me it can’t be true. I’m conscious of my surroundings all the time and yet I can’t believe them. You know, like when you are asleep and dream about something beautiful, and then wake up and find it’s actually true. I get a thrill out of just being here. ‘I’m crossing the campus,’ I say to myself. ‘I’m a student﻿—a girl student﻿—in Midwest University and now I’m crossing the campus of my university to go to a class.’ ”

===Chinese===
「你想象不到，能待在这里对我意味着什么……那么些年啊！我从前净梦见这个。就是现在，我还觉得这不可能是真的。我时时刻刻都清楚我周围的一切，可又不敢信。你知道，就像睡着时梦见了什么美事，一觉醒来发现它竟是真的。光是待在这儿，我心里就一阵阵发热。『我正走过校园呢，』我对自己说。『我是个学生——一个女学生——在中西部大学，此刻我正走过我的大学的校园，去上课。』」

===Original===
Her face was very greasy and earnest and fine.

===Chinese===
她的脸油腻腻的，恳切，而且美好。

===Original===
“Well, that’s great,” Dirk replied, weakly. “That’s cer’nly great.”

===Chinese===
「嗐，那敢情好，」德克答得软塌塌的。「那可真是好。」

===Original===
He told his mother about her. Usually he went home on Friday nights to stay until Monday morning. His first Monday-morning class was not until ten. Selina was deeply interested and stirred. “Do you think she’d spend some Saturday and Sunday here with us on the farm? She could come with you on Friday and go back Sunday night if she wanted to. Or stay until Monday morning and go back with you. There’s the spare room, all quiet and cool. She could do as she liked. I’d give her cream and all the fresh fruit and vegetables she wanted. And Meena would bake one of her fresh coconut cakes. I’d have Adam bring a fresh coconut from South Water Street.”

===Chinese===
他把她的情形讲给母亲听。他通常星期五晚上回家，住到星期一早上。他星期一的头一堂课要到十点才上。塞莉娜大感兴趣，心绪翻涌。「你看她肯不肯到咱们农场上来，同我们住一个周六礼拜天？她愿意的话，可以礼拜五跟你一块儿来，礼拜天夜里回去。要不就住到礼拜一早上，跟你一块儿走。有空着的客房，又清净又凉快。她爱怎样就怎样。奶油随她喝，新鲜水果蔬菜管她够。米娜还会给她烤她那种鲜椰子蛋糕。我会叫亚当去南水街捎一只新鲜椰子回来。」

===Original===
Mattie came one Friday night. It was the end of October, and Indian summer, the most beautiful time of the year on the Illinois prairie. A mellow golden light seemed to suffuse everything. It was as if the very air were liquid gold, and tonic. The squash and pumpkins next the good brown earth gave back the glow, and the frost-turned leaves of the maples in the sun. About the countryside for miles was the look of bounteousness, of plenty, of prophecy fulfilled as when a beautiful and fertile woman having borne her children and found them good, now sits serene-eyed, gracious, ample bosomed, satisfied.

===Chinese===
玛蒂在一个星期五晚上来了。那是十月末，正赶上小阳春（Indian summer）——伊利诺伊草原上一年里最美的时节。一种温醇的金光仿佛浸润了万物。空气本身就像是溶开的金子，又是提神的补剂。贴着褐色沃土的笋瓜和南瓜把这份光辉映还回去，经霜的枫叶在太阳底下也一样。方圆多少里的乡野，都是一副丰饶、富足、预言应验的模样，好比一个美丽多产的女人，生养了儿女，看着他们个个都好，如今目光安详、仪态雍容、胸脯丰满、心满意足地坐在那里。

===Original===
Into the face of Mattie Schwengauer there came a certain glory. When she and Selina clasped hands Selina stared at her rather curiously, as though startled. Afterward she said to Dirk, aside, “But I thought you said she was ugly!”

===Chinese===
玛蒂·施文高尔的脸上罩上了一层光彩。她和塞莉娜握手的时候，塞莉娜颇为好奇地盯着她看，像是吃了一惊。过后她把德克拉到一边说：「可我记得你说她长得丑来着！」

===Original===
“Well, she is, or﻿—well, isn’t she?”

===Chinese===
「呃，她是丑啊——呃——要不，她难道不丑吗？」

===Original===
“Look at her!”

===Chinese===
「你自己看她呀！」

===Original===
Mattie Schwengauer was talking to Meena Bras, the houseworker. She was standing with her hands on her ample hips, her fine head thrown back, her eyes alight, her lips smiling so that you saw her strong square teeth. A new cream separator was the subject of their conversation. Something had amused Mattie. She laughed. It was the laugh of a young girl, carefree, relaxed, at ease.

===Chinese===
玛蒂·施文高尔正同女工米娜·布拉斯说话。她双手叉着丰满的胯，清秀的头往后仰着，眼睛发亮，嘴唇含笑，笑得露出她那口方正结实的牙齿。两人谈的是一架新的奶油分离机。有什么把玛蒂逗乐了。她笑起来。那是年轻姑娘的笑，无忧无虑，舒展，自在。

===Original===
For two days Mattie did as she pleased, which meant she helped pull vegetables in the garden, milk the cows, saddle the horses; rode them without a saddle in the pasture. She tramped the road. She scuffled through the leaves in the woods, wore a scarlet maple leaf in her hair, slept like one gloriously dead from ten until six; ate prodigiously of cream, fruits, vegetables, eggs, sausage, cake.

===Chinese===
两天里玛蒂随心所欲，也就是说，她帮着园里拔菜，挤牛奶，给马上鞍；又不用鞍子骑着马在牧场上跑。她大步踏乡路。在树林子里踢踏着落叶走，头发上别一片猩红的枫叶，从十点一觉睡到六点，睡得像幸福地死过去一般；奶油、水果、蔬菜、鸡蛋、香肠、蛋糕，吃得海量。

===Original===
“It got so I hated to do all those things on the farm,” she said, laughing a little shamefacedly. “I guess it was because I had to. But now it comes back to me and I enjoy it because it’s natural to me, I suppose. Anyway, I’m having a grand time, Mrs. DeJong. The grandest time I ever had in my life.” Her face was radiant and almost beautiful.

===Chinese===
「从前在农场上，这些活儿我做到后来都恨了，」她说着，有点赧然地笑了。「我猜是因为不干不行吧。可现在那股劲儿又回来了，我干得高兴，大概是这本来就在我天性里。反正，我玩得痛快极了，德容太太。这辈子头一回这么痛快。」她的脸放着光，几乎是美的。

===Original===
“If you want me to believe that,” said Selina, “you’ll come again.”

===Chinese===
「你要是叫我相信这话，」塞莉娜说，「你就得再来。」

===Original===
But Mattie Schwengauer never did come again.

===Chinese===
可是玛蒂·施文高尔再也没有来过。

===Original===
Early the next week one of the university students approached Dirk. He was a Junior, very influential in his class, and a member of the fraternity to which Dirk was practically pledged. A decidedly desirable frat.

===Chinese===
下一周刚开头，大学里的一个学生找上德克。他是个三年级生，在班里很有势力，又是德克差不多已经内定要加入的那个兄弟会（fraternity）的成员。一个着实叫人眼热的兄弟会。

===Original===
“Say, look here, DeJong, I want to talk to you a minute. Uh, you’ve got to cut out that girl﻿—Swinegour or whatever her name is﻿—or it’s all off with the fellows in the frat.”

===Chinese===
「我说，你过来，德容，我跟你谈一分钟。呃，你得跟那丫头断了——猪猡高尔还是叫什么来着（Swinegour）——不然的话，兄弟会那帮哥们儿的事就算吹了。」

===Original===
“What d’you mean! Cut out! What’s the matter with her!”

===Chinese===
「你什么意思！断了！她怎么了！」

===Original===
“Matter! She’s Unclassified, isn’t she! And do you know what the story is? She told it herself as an economy hint to a girl who was working her way through. She bathes with her union suit and white stockings on to save laundry soap. Scrubs ’em on her! ’S the God’s truth.”

===Chinese===
「怎么了！她是非注册生，不是吗！还有，你知道外头怎么传的吗？这话是她自己当省钱窍门，讲给一个半工半读的姑娘听的。她洗澡时连衫裤（union suit）和白长袜都不脱，就为省洗衣皂。穿着一块儿搓！千真万确。」

===Original===
Into Dirk’s mind there flashed a picture of this large girl in her tight knitted union suit and her white stockings sitting in a tub half full of water and scrubbing them and herself simultaneously. A comic picture, and a revolting one. Pathetic, too, but he would not admit that.

===Chinese===
德克脑子里闪过一幅画面：这个高大的姑娘，穿着紧绷绷的针织连衫裤和白长袜，坐在半盆水里，衣裳和身子一块儿搓。一幅可笑的画面，也是一幅可憎的画面。也可怜——可他不肯承认这个。

===Original===
“Imagine!” the frat brother-to-be was saying. “Well, we can’t have a fellow who goes around with a girl like that. You got to cut her out, see! Completely. The fellahs won’t stand for it.”

===Chinese===
「你想想！」这位准兄弟会哥儿们说。「嗐，同这种丫头招摇过市的人，咱们兄弟会要不得。你得把她甩了，明白吗！干干净净地。哥儿们容不下这个。」

===Original===
Dirk had a mental picture of himself striking a noble attitude and saying, “Won’t stand for it, huh! She’s worth more than the whole caboodle of you put together. And you can all go to hell!”

===Chinese===
德克脑子里映出另一幅画面：自己凛然摆出一个高贵的姿态，说：「容不下，哼！她一个人比你们这一窝加在一起还值钱。你们统统见鬼去吧！」

===Original===
Instead he said, vaguely, “Oh. Well. Uh﻿—”

===Chinese===
可他实际说出口的，含含糊糊是：「哦。这个。呃——」

===Original===
Dirk changed his seat in the classroom, avoided Mattie’s eye, shot out of the door the minute class was over. One day he saw her coming toward him on the campus and he sensed that she intended to stop and speak to him﻿—chide him laughingly, perhaps. He quickened his pace, swerved a little to one side, and as he passed lifted his cap and nodded, keeping his eyes straight ahead. Out of the tail of his eye he could see her standing a moment irresolutely in the path.

===Chinese===
课堂上德克换了座位，避开玛蒂的目光，一下课就窜出门去。有一天他在校园里看见她朝自己走来，觉出她有意停下来说话——兴许还要笑着数落他几句。他加快脚步，稍稍偏到一边，擦身而过时举了举帽子点点头，眼睛笔直望着前方。从眼角里他看得见她在小径上怔怔地站了片刻。

===Original===
He got into the fraternity. The fellahs liked him from the first. Selina said once or twice, “Why don’t you bring that nice Mattie home with you again some time soon? Such a nice girl﻿—woman, rather. But she seemed so young and carefree while she was here, didn’t she? A fine mind, too, that girl. She’ll make something of herself. You’ll see. Bring her next week, h’m?”

===Chinese===
他进了兄弟会。哥儿们打一开始就喜欢他。塞莉娜说过一两回：「你怎么不挑个日子，把那个好姑娘玛蒂再带回家来？多好的姑娘——不如说，多好的女人。可她在这儿那阵子，显得多年轻、多没心事，是不是？那姑娘还有副好头脑。她会有出息的。你等着瞧。下礼拜带她来，好不好？」

===Original===
Dirk shuffled, coughed, looked away. “Oh, I dunno. Haven’t seen her lately. Guess she’s busy with another crowd, or something.”

===Chinese===
德克蹭着脚，咳嗽，眼睛望向别处。「哦，说不准。近来没见着她。大概跟另一帮人泡着呢，还是怎么的。」

===Original===
He tried not to think of what he had done, for he was honestly ashamed. Terribly ashamed. So he said to himself, “Oh, what of it!” and hid his shame. A month later Selina again said, “I wish you’d invite Mattie for Thanksgiving dinner. Unless she’s going home, which I doubt. We’ll have turkey and pumpkin pie and all the rest of it. She’ll love it.”

===Chinese===
他尽量不去想自己干下的事，因为他真心觉得羞愧。羞愧极了。于是他对自己说：「嗐，那又怎样！」把羞愧藏了起来。一个月后塞莉娜又说：「我盼着你请玛蒂来吃感恩节的正餐。除非她要回家——我看她多半没家可回。咱们有火鸡、南瓜馅饼，还有全套的吃食。她会喜欢的。」

===Original===
“Mattie?” He had actually forgotten her name.

===Chinese===
「玛蒂？」他竟把她的名字忘了。

===Original===
“Yes, of course. Isn’t that right? Mattie Schwengauer?”

===Chinese===
「是啊，当然。没记错吧？玛蒂·施文高尔？」

===Original===
“Oh, her. Uh﻿—well﻿—I haven’t been seeing her lately.”

===Chinese===
「哦，她呀。呃——这个——近来没大见着她。」

===Original===
“Oh, Dirk, you haven’t quarrelled with that nice girl!”

===Chinese===
「哦，德克，你不会是同那个好姑娘吵架了吧！」

===Original===
He decided to have it out. “Listen, Mother. There are a lot of different crowds at the U, see? And Mattie doesn’t belong to any of ’em. You wouldn’t understand, but it’s like this. She﻿—she’s smart and jolly and everything but she just doesn’t belong. Being friends with a girl like that doesn’t get you anywhere. Besides, she isn’t a girl. She’s a middle-aged woman, when you come to think of it.”

===Chinese===
他决定把话说明白。「你听着，妈妈。大学里有好些个不同的圈子，明白吗？玛蒂哪个圈子都不沾边。你是不会懂的，可事情就是这样。她——她又聪明又快活，什么都好，可她就是不合群。同这样的姑娘做朋友，不会有出路。再说，她也不是姑娘了。认真说来，她是个中年女人。」

===Original===
“Doesn’t get you anywhere!” Selina’s tone was cool and even. Then, as the boy’s gaze did not meet hers: “Why, Dirk DeJong, Mattie Schwengauer is one of my reasons for sending you to a university. She’s what I call part of a university education. Just talking to her is learning something valuable. I don’t mean that you wouldn’t naturally prefer pretty young girls of your own age to go around with, and all. It would be queer if you didn’t. But this Mattie﻿—why, she’s life. Do you remember that story of when she washed dishes in the kosher restaurant over on Twelfth Street and the proprietor used to rent out dishes and cutlery for Irish and Italian neighbourhood weddings where they had pork and goodness knows what all, and then use them next day in the restaurant again for the kosher customers?”

===Chinese===
「不会有出路！」塞莉娜的声调又冷又平。随后，见儿子的目光不肯同她的相接：「嗐，德克·德容，玛蒂·施文高尔就是我送你上大学的理由之一。她这样的人，依我说，本身就是大学教育的一部分。光是同她说说话，就是在学宝贵的东西。我不是说你自然而然不会更喜欢带着同龄的漂亮姑娘四处走走，等等。你若不那样，倒怪了。可这个玛蒂——嗐，她就是生活本身。你还记得她讲的那个故事吗——她在十二街那家犹太洁食（kosher）餐馆里洗盘子，老板惯常把盘碟刀叉租出去给街坊爱尔兰人、意大利人的婚宴用——那上头有猪肉，天知道还有些什么——第二天再收回来，接着伺候洁食的顾客？」

===Original===
Yes, Dirk remembered. Selina wrote Mattie, inviting her to the farm for Thanksgiving, and Mattie answered gratefully, declining. “I shall always remember you,” she wrote in that letter, “with love.”

===Chinese===
是的，德克记得。塞莉娜给玛蒂写了信，请她来农场过感恩节，玛蒂满怀感激地回了信，谢绝了。「我会永远记得您，」她在那封信里写道，「怀着爱。」

## XIV / 第十四章

===Original===
Throughout Dirk’s Freshman year there were, for him, no heartening, informal, mellow talks before the wood-fire in the book-lined study of some professor whose wisdom was such a mixture of classic lore and modernism as to be an inspiration to his listeners. Midwest professors delivered their lectures in the classroom as they had been delivering them in the past ten or twenty years and as they would deliver them until death or a trustees’ meeting should remove them. The younger professors and instructors in natty gray suits and bright-coloured ties made a point of being unpedantic in the classroom and rather overdid it. They posed as being one of the fellows; would dashingly use a bit of slang to create a laugh from the boys and an adoring titter from the girls. Dirk somehow preferred the pedants to these. When these had to give an informal talk to the men before some university event they would start by saying, “Now listen, fellahs﻿—” At the dances they were not above “rushing” the pretty co-eds.

===Chinese===
德克在大一整个学年里，从没享受过什么令人振奋的、不拘形迹的、炉边闲话式的谈话——在某位教授四壁藏书的书房里，坐在柴炉前听他娓娓而谈，那教授的智慧熔古典学问与现代思想于一炉，足以启迪听者。中西部大学的教授们照旧在课堂上讲他们的课，一如过去十年二十年那样讲，而且还要一直讲到死神或者校董会议把他们撤下来为止。年轻些的教授和讲师们，衣着入时——灰西装、鲜亮领带——特意在课堂上摆出不迂腐的架势，未免做得过火。他们装作哥儿们中间的一个；会大大咧咧甩出一句俚语，博男生们一场大笑、女生们爱慕的吃吃窃笑。不知怎的，德克宁可要那些迂夫子，也不喜欢这一路人物。每逢大学的某项活动之前，这班人得对男学生们来一番非正式讲话，开口便是：「都听着，弟兄们——」舞会上，他们也不惜向漂亮的女同学大献殷勤。

===Original===
Two of Dirk’s classes were conducted by women professors. They were well on toward middle age, or past it; desiccated women. Only their eyes were alive. Their clothes were of some indefinite dark stuff, brown or drab-gray; their hair lifeless; their hands long, bony, unvital. They had seen classes and classes and classes. A roomful of fresh young faces that appeared briefly only to be replaced by another roomful of fresh young faces like round white pencil marks manipulated momentarily on a slate, only to be sponged off to give way to other round white marks. Of the two women one﻿—the elder﻿—was occasionally likely to flare into sudden life; a flame in the ashes of a burned-out grate. She had humour and a certain caustic wit, qualities that had managed miraculously to survive even the deadly and numbing effects of thirty years in the classroom. A fine mind, and iconoclastic, hampered by the restrictions of a conventional community and the soul of a congenital spinster.

===Chinese===
德克有两门课是由女教授执教的。两人都已年近中年，或者已经过了中年；是干瘪枯槁的女人，只有一双眼睛还活着。衣裳是不明不确的深色料子，棕色或灰褐色；头发没有生气；一双手又长又瘦，毫无血色。她们见过的班级一届又一届，又一届，又一届。满满一屋新鲜年轻的脸孔短暂地出现，随即被另一满满一屋新鲜年轻的脸孔取代，就像石板上临时划下的一颗颗白色圆粉笔印，转眼被擦去，让位给别的白圈圈。这两位女教授里头，年长的那位偶尔会忽然迸出一点生气；犹如熄透的炉格里的一星火苗。她有幽默感，还有一种刻薄的机智——这两种品质，历经三十年课堂的致命麻醉，居然奇迹般地幸存了下来。一副优秀的头脑，一副离经叛道的脾性，却被因循守旧的社区的种种清规戒律捆着，又生就一颗天生的老处女的灵魂。

===Original===
Under the guidance of these Dirk chafed and grew restless. Miss Euphemia Hollingswood had a way of emphasizing every third or fifth syllable, bringing her voice down hard on it, thus:

===Chinese===
在这样的导师引领之下，德克焦躁难安。尤菲米娅·霍林斯伍德小姐（Euphemia Hollingswood）有个习惯，每隔三五个音节就要重读一下，嗓音狠狠砸在那个音节上，就像这样：

===Original===
“In the *con*sideration of *all* the facts in the *case* presented be*fore* us we must *first* review the *his*tory and at*tempt* to analyze the *out*standing﻿—”

===Chinese===
「在对*呈*交到我们面前的本案*全*部*事*实加以*审*酌之际，我们必*须*首*先回顾其*历*史，并*尝*试剖析其中*突*出的问题——」

===Original===
He found himself waiting for that emphasis and shrinking from it as from a sledgehammer blow. It hurt his head.

===Chinese===
他发现自己竟在等着那一记重读，又像躲大锤的锤击一样躲着它。那砸得他头疼。

===Original===
Miss Lodge droned. She approached a word with a maddening uh-uh-uh-uh. In the uh-uh-uh face of the uh-uh-uh-uh geometrical situation of the uh-uh-uh uh﻿—

===Chinese===
洛奇小姐（Lodge）讲课嗡嗡如念经。逼近一个词之前，先来一阵叫人发疯的呃、呃、呃、呃。「在呃、呃、呃面对呃、呃、呃、呃之几何态势时的呃、呃、呃呃——」

===Original===
He shifted restlessly in his chair, found his hands clenched into fists, and took refuge in watching the shadow cast by an oak branch outside the window on a patch of sunlight against the blackboard behind her.

===Chinese===
他在椅子里不安地挪动，发觉自己双手攥成了拳头，只好借看着窗外一根橡树枝投在她身后黑板上一片阳光里的影子来逃遁。

===Original===
During the early spring Dirk and Selina talked things over again, seated before their own fireplace in the High Prairie farmhouse. Selina had had that fireplace built five years before and her love of it amounted to fire-worship. She had it lighted always on winter evenings and in the spring when the nights were sharp. In Dirk’s absence she would sit before it at night long after the rest of the weary household had gone to bed. Old Pom, the mongrel, lay stretched at her feet enjoying such luxury in old age as he had never dreamed of in his bastard youth. High Prairie, driving by from some rare social gathering or making a late trip to market as they sometimes were forced to do, saw the rosy flicker of Mrs. DeJong’s fire dancing on the wall and warmed themselves by it even while they resented it.

===Chinese===
早春时节，德克和塞莉娜又一次坐在高草原农舍自家的壁炉前商谈事情。那座壁炉是塞莉娜五年前叫人砌的，她对它的爱近乎拜火。冬天的傍晚，还有春夜寒峭的时候，炉火总是生着。德克不在家时，等疲惫的一家人都上床睡了许久，她还独自在夜里坐在炉前。老波姆那条杂种狗伸直了趴在她脚边，享受着晚年的这份奢侈——它那杂种小子的时候做梦也没梦到过的奢侈。高草原的人家，赶完难得的社交聚会回家，或者像他们有时被迫的那样深更半夜运菜进城，路过时望见德容太太家的炉火映在墙上跳动的玫瑰色光焰，嘴上心里不忿，身子却也被它烘暖了。

===Original===
“A good heater in there and yet anyway she’s got to have a fire going in a grate. Always she does something funny like that. I should think she’d be lonesome sitting there like that with her dog only.”

===Chinese===
「屋里明明有好火炉，她偏还要在壁炉格里生火。她老是干这种怪事儿。一个人就守着条狗那么坐着，我还只当她不闷得慌呢。」

===Original===
They never knew how many guests Selina entertained there before her fire those winter evenings﻿—old friends and new. Sobig was there, the plump earth-grimed baby who rolled and tumbled in the fields while his young mother wiped the sweat from her face to look at him with fond eyes. Dirk DeJong of ten years hence was there. Simeon Peake, dapper, soft-spoken, ironic, in his shiny boots and his hat always a little on one side. Pervus DeJong, a blue-shirted giant with strong tender hands and little fine golden hairs on the backs of them. Fanny Davenport, the actress-idol of her girlhood came back to her, smiling, bowing; and the gorgeous spangled creatures in the tights and bodices of the old Extravaganzas. In strange contrast to these was the patient, tireless figure of Maartje Pool standing in the doorway of Roelf’s little shed, her arms tucked in her apron for warmth. “You make fun, huh?” she said, wistfully, “you and Roelf. You make fun.” And Roelf, the dark vivid boy, misunderstood. Roelf, the genius. He was always one of the company.

===Chinese===
他们从不知道，那些冬夜塞莉娜在炉火前款待过多少客人——旧雨新知。「这么大」在那里：那个胖乎乎、沾着泥土的娃娃，在田里打滚撒欢，他年轻的母亲抹一把脸上的汗水，用疼爱的眼光望着他。十年后的德克·德容也在那里。西缅·皮克，衣冠楚楚，言语温和，语带讥诮，锃亮的靴子，帽子永远稍稍歪向一边。珀维斯·德容，穿蓝衬衫的巨人，一双有力而温柔的手，手背上生着细细的金色茸毛。少女时代崇拜的偶像、女演员范妮·达文波特回到她身边，微笑着，欠身施礼；还有旧日奇幻剧里那些穿紧身衣裤、缀满亮片的华服人物。与这些形成奇异对照的，是玛尔切·普尔那耐心而不知疲倦的身影，站在鲁尔夫那间小棚屋门口，两臂拢进围裙里取暖。「你们逗乐子，嗯？」她怅然说道，「你跟鲁尔夫。你们逗乐子。」还有鲁尔夫，那个黑瘦鲜活的男孩，被人误解的鲁尔夫。鲁尔夫，那个天才。他永远是座中客之一。

===Original===
Oh, Selina DeJong never was lonely on these winter evenings before her fire.

===Chinese===
哦，这样的冬夜，塞莉娜·德容在炉火前从来不曾孤单过。

===Original===
She and Dirk sat there one fine sharp evening in early April. It was Saturday. Of late Dirk had not always come to the farm for the weekend. Eugene and Paula Arnold had been home for the Easter holidays. Julie Arnold had invited Dirk to the gay parties at the Prairie Avenue house. He had even spent two entire weekends there. After the brocaded luxury of the Prairie Avenue house his farm bedroom seemed almost startlingly stark and bare. Selina frankly enjoyed Dirk’s somewhat fragmentary accounts of these visits; extracted from them as much vicarious pleasure as he had had in the reality﻿—more, probably.

===Chinese===
四月初一个晴朗料峭的傍晚，她和德克坐在那里。那天是星期六。近来德克周末并不总回农场来。尤金和宝拉·阿诺德回家过了复活节。朱莉·阿诺德请德克去草原大道的宅子参加热闹的宴会。他甚至在那里度过了整整两个周末。见识过草原大道宅子里锦缎般的豪奢之后，他农场上的卧房显得简直出奇地寒碜空落。塞莉娜对德克讲述这些做客经历的断简残篇倒也坦然受用；从里面榨出的间接快乐，不亚于他亲身所得——多半还更浓些。

===Original===
“Now tell me what you had to eat,” she would say, sociably, like a child. “What did you have for dinner, for example? Was it grand? Julie tells me they have a butler now. Well! I can’t wait till I hear Aug Hempel on the subject.”

===Chinese===
「快跟我说说你们吃了什么，」她会像个孩子似的，兴兴头头地说。「比方说，正餐吃的什么？排场大吗？朱莉告诉我他们家如今雇了管家男仆了。嘿！我等不及要听奥格·亨佩尔对这件事怎么说了。」

===Original===
He would tell her of the grandeurs of the Arnold ménage. She would interrupt and exclaim: “Mayonnaise! On fruit! Oh, I don’t believe I’d like *that*. You did! Well, I’ll have it for you next week when you come home. I’ll get the recipe from Julie.”

===Chinese===
他便给她讲阿诺德家的种种气派。她不断插嘴惊叹：「蛋黄酱！浇在水果上！哎呀，我看我不会喜欢*那个*的。你倒喜欢！好吧，下星期你回家，我给你做。我找朱莉要个方子。」

===Original===
He didn’t think he’d be home next week. One of the fellows he’d met at the Arnolds’ had invited him to their place out north, on the lake. He had a boat.

===Chinese===
他想下星期恐怕回不了家。在阿诺德家结识的一个小伙子请他去北边湖上他们家里做客。那家有条船。

===Original===
