“That’ll be lovely!” Selina exclaimed, after an almost unnoticeable moment of silence﻿—silence with panic in it. “I’ll try not to fuss and be worried like an old hen every minute of the time I think you’re on the water.﻿ ﻿… Now do go on, Sobig. First fruit with mayonnaise, h’m? What kind of soup?”

===Chinese===
「那太好了！」塞莉娜叫道，此前有一段几乎察觉不到的沉默——藏着恐慌的沉默。「我会尽量不咋咋呼呼，不学老母鸡，一想到你在水上就一分钟一揪心。……接着讲呀，「这么大」。先上的水果浇蛋黄酱，嗯？汤是什么汤？」

===Original===
He was not a naturally talkative person. There was nothing surly about his silence. It was a taciturn streak inherited from his Dutch ancestry. This time, though, he was more voluble than usual. “Paula﻿ ﻿…” came again and again into his conversation. “Paula﻿ ﻿… Paula﻿ ﻿…” and again “… Paula.” He did not seem conscious of the repetition, but Selina’s quick ear caught it.

===Chinese===
他天生不是个多话的人。他的沉默里没有乖戾的成分。那是从荷兰血统里继承来的一条寡言的筋。可这一晚，他比平常健谈。「宝拉……」一次又一次溜进他的话里。「宝拉……宝拉……」又是一句「……宝拉」。他自己似乎不觉得这个重复，塞莉娜敏锐的耳朵却听出来了。

===Original===
“I haven’t seen her,” Selina said, “since she went away to school the first year. She must be﻿—let’s see﻿—she’s a year older than you are. She’s nineteen going on twenty. Last time I saw her I thought she was a dark scrawny little thing. Too bad she didn’t inherit Julie’s lovely gold colouring and good looks, instead of Eugene, who doesn’t need ’em.”

===Chinese===
「她上学去的第一年起，我就没见过她了，」塞莉娜说，「算起来——她比你大一岁。她十九了，快二十。上回见她，我记得是个黑瘦干瘪的小东西。可惜朱莉那身可爱的金颜色和好相貌没传给她，倒传给了尤金——他又用不着。」

===Original===
“She isn’t!” said Dirk, hotly. “She’s dark and slim and sort of﻿—uh﻿—sensuous”﻿—Selina started visibly, and raised her hand quickly to her mouth to hide a smile﻿—“like Cleopatra. Her eyes are big and kind of slanting﻿—not squinty I don’t mean, but slanting up a little at the corners. Cut out, kind of, so that they look bigger than most people’s.”

===Chinese===
「她才不是！」德克激动地说。「她是黑，可苗条，还挺——呃——肉感的」——塞莉娜明显一怔，赶忙抬手掩住嘴角的笑意——「像克娄巴特拉。她眼睛大大的，有点儿吊——不是斜眼，我不是那个意思，是眼角微微往上挑。眼窝像是剜出来的，所以显得比一般人的大。」

===Original===
“My eyes used to be considered rather fine,” said Selina, mischievously; but he did not hear.

===Chinese===
「我这双眼睛，从前也算得上出色的，」塞莉娜促狭地说；可他没听见。

===Original===
“She makes all the other girls look sort of blowzy.” He was silent a moment. Selina was silent, too, and it was not a happy silence. Dirk spoke again, suddenly, as though continuing aloud a train of thought, “﻿—all but her hands.”

===Chinese===
「别的姑娘跟她一比，都显得邋遢粗蠢。」他沉默了一会儿。塞莉娜也沉默着，那可不是一段快活的沉默。德克忽然又开了口，像是把心里盘算的一段思路大声接了下去：「——就是那双手除外。」

===Original===
Selina made her voice sound natural, not sharply inquisitive. “What’s the matter with her hands, Dirk?”

===Chinese===
塞莉娜把嗓音放得自然，不带刨根问底的锋芒。「她的手怎么啦，德克？」

===Original===
He pondered a moment, his brows knitted. At last, slowly, “Well, I don’t know. They’re brown, and awfully thin and sort of﻿—grabby. I mean it makes me nervous to watch them. And when the rest of her is cool they’re hot when you touch them.”

===Chinese===
他皱着眉头思量了一会儿。末了，慢慢地说：「呃，我也说不上。它们是褐色的，瘦得厉害，还挺——抓攫攫的。我是说，看着它们我就紧张。她浑身凉的时候，那双手摸上去却是烫的。」

===Original===
He looked at his mother’s hands that were busy with some sewing. The stuff on which she was working was a bit of satin ribbon; part of a hood intended to grace the head of Geertje Pool Vander Sijde’s second baby. She had difficulty in keeping her rough fingers from catching on the soft surface of the satin. Manual work, water, sun, and wind had tanned those hands, hardened them, enlarged the knuckles, spread them, roughened them. Yet how sure they were, and strong, and cool and reliable﻿—and tender. Suddenly, looking at them, Dirk said, “Now your hands. I love your hands, Mother.”

===Chinese===
他望着母亲那双正忙着针线活的手。她手上做的是一截缎带，是一顶婴儿软帽上的缀饰，那帽子要给海尔切·普尔·范德赛德的第二个娃娃的小脑袋增色。她粗糙的手指总挂住缎子柔滑的表面，得时时当心。体力活、水、日头和风，把那双手晒黑了，磨硬了，磨大了骨节，撑宽了骨架，磨粗了皮肉。然而它们多么稳当，多么有力，多么清凉可靠——又多么温柔。德克忽然望着它们开了口：「看你这双手。我爱你这双手，妈妈。」

===Original===
She put down her work hastily, yet quietly, so that the sudden rush of happy grateful tears in her eyes should not sully the pink satin ribbon. She was flushed, like a girl. “Do you, Sobig?” she said.

===Chinese===
她赶忙放下针线，却放得轻轻的，生怕眼里猛然涌上的、快乐感激的泪珠弄脏了那截粉红缎带。她脸红了，像个小姑娘。「是吗，「这么大」？」她说。

===Original===
After a moment she took up her sewing again. Her face looked young, eager, fresh, like the face of the girl who had found cabbages so beautiful that night when she bounced along the rutty Halsted road with Klaas Pool, many years ago. It came into her face, that look, when she was happy, exhilarated, excited. That was why those who loved her and brought that look into her face thought her beautiful, while those who did not love her never saw the look and consequently considered her a plain woman.

===Chinese===
过了一会儿，她又拿起针线。她的脸显得年轻、热切、新鲜，像许多年前那个夜晚、颠簸在坑坑洼洼的哈尔斯特德路上、发现卷心菜竟那么美的那个姑娘的脸。每逢她快活、振奋、激动的时候，那副神采就会浮上她的脸。所以爱她的人——能引出这副神采的人——觉得她美；不爱她的人从没见过这副神采，于是把她看作一个长相平常的女人。

===Original===
There was another silence between the two. Then: “Mother, what would you think of my going East next fall, to take a course in architecture?”

===Chinese===
母子间又是一阵沉默。然后：「妈妈，要是我明年秋天上东部去念建筑学，你怎么想？」

===Original===
“Would you like that, Dirk?”

===Chinese===
「你喜欢吗，德克？」

===Original===
“Yes, I think so﻿—yes.”

===Chinese===
「喜欢，我想是的——喜欢。」

===Original===
“Then I’d like it better than anything in the world. I﻿—it makes me happy just to think of it.”

===Chinese===
「那我就比世上什么都乐意。我——光是想想这件事，心里就快活。」

===Original===
“It would﻿—cost an awful lot.”

===Chinese===
「那可要——花好大一笔钱。」

===Original===
“I’ll manage. I’ll manage.﻿ ﻿… What made you decide on architecture?”

===Chinese===
「我来想办法。我来想办法。……你怎么想起学建筑的？」

===Original===
“I don’t know, exactly. The new buildings at the university﻿—Gothic, you know﻿—are such a contrast to the old. Then Paula and I were talking the other day. She hates their house on Prairie﻿—terrible old lumpy gray stone pile, with the black of the I.C. trains all over it. She wants her father to build north﻿—an Italian villa or French château. Something of that sort. So many of her friends are moving to the north shore, away from these hideous south-side and north-side Chicago houses with their stoops, and their bay windows, and their terrible turrets. Ugh!”

===Chinese===
「我也说不确切。学校的新楼——哥特式的，你知道——跟老楼一比，差别太大了。再就是前两天我跟宝拉聊天来着。她讨厌他们草原大道上的房子——一座疙里疙瘩的灰色石头老堆，让伊利诺伊中央（I.C.）的列车熏得浑身乌黑。她想要她爸爸到北边去盖新宅——意大利别墅，或者法国城堡，那一类的东西。她的朋友好些都搬到北湖滨去了，躲开芝加哥城南城北这些丑房子：门口的台阶啦，凸窗啦，还有那些要命的角楼。呸！」

===Original===
“Well, now, do you know,” Selina remonstrated mildly, “I like ’em. I suppose I’m wrong, but to me they seem sort of natural and solid and unpretentious, like the clothes that old August Hempel wears, so square-cut and baggy. Those houses look dignified to me, and fitting. They may be ugly﻿—probably are﻿—but anyway they’re not ridiculous. They have a certain rugged grandeur. They’re Chicago. Those French and Italian gimcracky things they﻿—they’re incongruous. It’s as if Abraham Lincoln were to appear suddenly in pink satin knee breeches and buckled shoes, and lace ruffles at his wrists.”

===Chinese===
「嗳，我说，你知道吗，」塞莉娜温和地抗辩道，「我就喜欢那样的房子。就算我不对吧，可在我眼里，它们显得自然、结实、不装模作样，就像老奥古斯特·亨佩尔穿的衣裳，方方正正，宽宽大大。那些房子在我看来有气派，相宜。它们也许丑——多半是丑——可至少不滑稽。它们自有一种粗砺的庄严。它们是芝加哥。那些法国意大利的花哨玩意儿——它们不搭调。那就像亚伯拉罕·林肯忽然穿上了粉缎及膝短裤、带扣襻的鞋，手腕上还翻了花边褶子。」

===Original===
Dirk could laugh at that picture. But he protested, too. “But there’s no native architecture, so what’s to be done! You wouldn’t call those smoke-blackened old stone and brick piles with their iron fences and their conservatories and cupolas and gingerbread exactly native, would you?”

===Chinese===
德克听了这幅图景笑了。但他也抗辩。「可本地根本没有本土建筑，那可怎么办！你总不会把那些烟熏火燎的老石头老砖堆——铁栏杆、暖花房、穹顶阁、满身花饰——也叫做本土的吧？」

===Original===
“No,” Selina admitted, “but those Italian villas and French châteaux in north Chicago suburbs are a good deal like a lace evening gown in the Arizona desert. It wouldn’t keep you cool in the daytime, and it wouldn’t be warm enough at night. I suppose a native architecture is evolved from building for the local climate and the needs of the community, keeping beauty in mind as you go. We don’t need turrets and towers any more than we need drawbridges and moats. It’s all right to keep them, I suppose, where they grew up, in a country where the feudal system meant that any day your next-door neighbour might take it into his head to call his gang around him and sneak up to steal your wife and tapestries and gold drinking cups.”

===Chinese===
「不会，」塞莉娜承认，「可芝加哥北郊那些意大利别墅、法国城堡，就跟亚利桑那沙漠里的一件蕾丝晚礼服差不多。白天不散热，夜里又不保暖。照我想，本土建筑是从当地的气候和居民的需要里慢慢长出来的，一路走一路把美放在心上。我们用不着角楼塔楼，就像用不着吊桥和护城河。留着它们当然也不碍事——在它们土生土长的地方，在那个封建制度意味着隔壁邻舍随时可能起了歹念、纠集一伙人摸过来、抢走你的老婆、你的挂毯和你喝酒的金杯的国度里。」

===Original===
Dirk was interested and amused. Talks with his mother were likely to affect him thus. “What’s your idea of a real Chicago house, Mother?”

===Chinese===
德克听得又有趣又入神。跟母亲谈话常有这样的效果。「那你心目中真正的芝加哥房子是什么样的，妈妈？」

===Original===
Selina answered quickly, as if she had thought often about it; as if she would have liked just such a dwelling on the site of the old DeJong farmhouse in which they now were seated so comfortably. “Well, it would need big porches for the hot days and nights so’s to catch the prevailing southwest winds from the prairies in the summer﻿—a porch that would be swung clear around to the east, too﻿—or a terrace or another porch east so that if the precious old lake breeze should come up just when you think you’re dying of the heat, as it sometimes does, you could catch that, too. It ought to be built﻿—the house, I mean﻿—rather squarish and tight and solid against our cold winters and northeasters. Then sleeping porches, of course. There’s a grand American institution for you! England may have its afternoon tea on the terrace, and Spain may have its patio, and France its courtyard, and Italy its pergola, vine-covered; but America’s got the sleeping porch﻿—the screened-in open-air sleeping porch, and I shouldn’t wonder if the man who first thought of that would get precedence, on Judgment Day, over the men who invented the aeroplane, the talking machine, and the telephone. After all, he had nothing in mind but the health of the human race.” After which grand period Selina grinned at Dirk, and Dirk grinned at Selina and the two giggled together there by the fireplace, companionably.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜立刻答了上来，仿佛这个问题她早已想过千百遍；仿佛她真想在德容家老农舍的地基上、在他们此刻坐得舒舒服服的这所房子的原址，盖起那么一座住宅。「嗯，大热天大热夜得有大游廊，好兜住夏天草原上常刮的西南风——游廊还得一路转到东边去——或者东面来一座露台，再不然再来个游廊，这样等那阵金贵的湖风、在你觉得自己快热死的时候忽然刮起来——它有时就是会这样——你也能兜个正着。房子呢——我是说房子本身——该造得方方正正、严严实实、结结实实，好对付咱们的寒冬和东北风暴。再就是睡廊了，这自然少不得。瞧，这才是个堂堂的美国制度！英格兰有露台上的午后茶，西班牙有天井，法兰西有庭院，意大利有爬满藤蔓的绿廊；可美国有睡廊——围了纱窗的露天睡廊——要我说，到了最后审判日，头一个想出这主意的人，排队该排在发明飞机、留声机和电话的那几位前头。毕竟，他心里想的，无非是全人类的健康。」这番高论发完，塞莉娜朝德克咧嘴一笑，德克朝塞莉娜咧嘴一笑，娘儿俩就着壁炉，亲亲热热地笑作一团。

===Original===
“Mother, you’re simply wonderful!﻿—only your native Chicago dwelling seems to be mostly porch.”

===Chinese===
「妈妈，你真是了不起！——只是你这本土芝加哥住宅，好像大半都是游廊。」

===Original===
Selina waved such carping criticism away with a careless hand. “Oh, well, any house that has enough porches, and two or three bathrooms and at least eight closets can be lived in comfortably, no matter what else it has or hasn’t got.”

===Chinese===
塞莉娜把手一挥，把这种吹毛求疵掸到一边。「嗳，行了，只要游廊够多，再有两三间浴室、至少八个壁橱，随便什么房子住着都舒坦——别的有什么没什么都无所谓。」

===Original===
Next day they were more serious. The eastern college and the architectural career seemed to be settled things. Selina was content, happy. Dirk was troubled about the expense. He spoke of it at breakfast next morning (Dirk’s breakfast; his mother had had hers hours before and now as he drank his coffee, was sitting with him a moment and glancing at the paper that had come in the rural mail delivery). She had been out in the fields overseeing the transplanting of young tomato seedlings from hotbed to field. She wore an old gray sweater buttoned up tight, for the air was still sharp. On her head was a battered black felt soft hat (an old one of Dirk’s) much like the one she had worn to the Haymarket that day ten years ago. Selina’s cheeks were faintly pink from her walk across the fields in the brisk morning air.

===Chinese===
第二天，他们的谈话严肃了些。上东部的大学、学建筑的前程，似乎都定下来了。塞莉娜心满意足，快快活活。德克却为花销发愁。第二天早晨吃早饭时他提起这事（这是德克的早饭；他母亲那顿几个钟头前就吃过了，如今他喝着咖啡，她陪坐片刻，翻看着乡村邮班送来的报纸）。她方才在地里督工，把番茄幼苗从温床移栽到大田。她穿一件扣得严严实实的旧灰毛衣，因为天气还挺冷。头上扣一顶磨得不成样子的黑毡软帽（德克的一顶旧帽子），跟十年前她上海马克特那天戴的那顶很相像。清晨清冽的空气里走了几趟地，塞莉娜的脸颊微微透出红来。

===Original===
She sniffed. “That coffee smells wonderful. I think I’ll just﻿—” She poured herself a half cup with the air of virtue worn by one who really longs for a whole cup and doesn’t take it.

===Chinese===
她吸了吸鼻子。「这咖啡真香。我想我就——」她给自己倒了半杯，带着那种一心想喝一整杯、却只倒半杯的人的美德神气。

===Original===
“I’ve been thinking,” he began, “the expense﻿—”

===Chinese===
「我在想，」他开口道，「那笔花销——」

===Original===
“Pigs,” said Selina, serenely.

===Chinese===
「猪，」塞莉娜安详地说。

===Original===
“Pigs!” He looked around, bewildered; stared at his mother.

===Chinese===
「猪！」他莫名其妙地四下张望；瞪着母亲。

===Original===
“Pigs’ll do it,” Selina explained, calmly. “I’ve been wanting to put them in for three or four years. It’s August Hempel’s idea. Hogs, I should have said.”

===Chinese===
「猪就办得成，」塞莉娜心平气和地解释。「这三四年我一直想养来着。这是奥古斯特·亨佩尔出的主意。对了，我该说肥猪（hogs）。」

===Original===
Again, as before, he echoed, “Hogs!” rather faintly.

===Chinese===
他又像方才一样，有气无力地应了一句：「肥猪！」

===Original===
“High-bred hogs. They’re worth their weight in silver this minute, and will be for years to come. I won’t go in for them extensively. Just enough to make an architect out of Mr. Dirk DeJong.” Then, at the expression in his face: “Don’t look so pained, son. There’s nothing revolting about a hog﻿—not my kind, brought up in a pen as sanitary as a tiled bathroom and fed on corn. He’s a handsome, impressive-looking animal, the hog, when he isn’t treated like one.”

===Chinese===
「良种肥猪。这会儿它们就值等身的银子，往后好些年都一样。我不打算大养。养到够把德克·德容先生造就成一位建筑师就行了。」接着，看见他脸上的神情：「别那么一脸苦相，儿子。一头猪没有什么叫人恶心的——我养的那种没有：猪圈干净得跟铺瓷砖的浴室一样，喂的是玉米。猪本是种漂亮的、有气派的牲口——只要不把它当猪来对待。」

===Original===
He looked dejected. “I’d rather not go to school on﻿—hogs.”

===Chinese===
他垂头丧气。「我不愿意靠——靠肥猪去上学。」

===Original===
She took off the felt hat and tossed it over to the old couch by the window; smoothed her hair back with the flat of her palm. You saw that the soft dark hair was liberally sprinkled with gray now, but the eyes were bright and clear as ever.

===Chinese===
她摘下毡帽，朝窗边那张旧长沙发扔过去；用手掌心把头发抿向脑后。你看得出，那头乌黑的软发如今已斑斑驳驳掺了不少灰白，可那双眼睛还跟从前一样明亮清澈。

===Original===
“You know, Sobig, this is what they call a paying farm﻿—as vegetable farms go. We’re out of debt, the land’s in good shape, the crop promises well if we don’t have another rainy cold spring like last year’s. But no truck garden is going to make its owner rich these days, with labour so high and the market what it is, and the expense of hauling and all. Any truck farmer who comes out even thinks he’s come out ahead.”

===Chinese===
「你是知道的，「这么大」，咱们这农场，人家管它叫赚钱的农场——在菜园行里来说。咱们的债还清了，地力养得好，只要别再碰上去年那样阴雨寒冷的春天，收成有望。可如今工钱这么贵，行市又是那样，加上运货的花销种种，哪个菜园也发不了财。做菜农的能不亏本，就觉得自己是赚了。」

===Original===
“I know it.” Rather miserably.

===Chinese===
「这我知道。」语气好不凄惶。

===Original===
“Well. I’m not complaining, son. I’m just telling you. I’m having a grand time. When I see the asparagus plantation actually yielding, that I planted ten years ago, I’m as happy as if I’d stumbled on a gold mine. I think, sometimes, of the way your father objected to my planting the first one. April, like this, in the country, with everything coming up green and new in the rich black loam﻿—I can’t tell you. And when I know that it goes to market as food﻿—the best kind of food, that keeps people’s bodies clean and clear and flexible and strong! I like to think of babies’ mothers saying: ‘Now eat your spinach, every scrap, or you can’t have any dessert!﻿ ﻿… Carrots make your eyes bright.﻿ ﻿… Finish your potato. Potatoes make you strong!’ ”

===Chinese===
「好了。我不是叫苦，儿子。我只是说给你听。我过得美着呢。看到我十年前种下的芦笋地真出了菜，我就像白捡了一座金矿那么快活。有时候我想起你父亲当初拦着我不叫种头一畦的架势。四月，就像现在，在乡下，肥黑的地里样样东西冒出又绿又新的芽——那滋味我说不出来。再想到这些菜当粮食进了市场——顶好的粮食，吃的人身子干净、通泰、活络、结实！我爱想那些当妈妈的对小娃娃说：『菠菜要吃干净，一口不剩，不然不许吃甜点心！……胡萝卜吃了眼睛亮。……土豆吃完。土豆吃了长力气！』」

===Original===
Selina laughed, flushed a little.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜笑了，微微红了脸。

===Original===
“Yes, but how about hogs? Do you feel that way about hogs?”

===Chinese===
「可肥猪呢？你对肥猪也有这份热乎劲儿？」

===Original===
“Certainly!” said Selina, briskly. She pushed toward him a little blue-and-white platter that lay on the white cloth near her elbow. “Have a bit more bacon, Dirk. One of these nice curly slivers that are so crisp.”

===Chinese===
「当然有！」塞莉娜爽脆地说。她把肘边白桌布上的一只蓝白小盘朝他推了推。「再吃点儿腌猪肉吧，德克。挑一条脆生生的卷边薄片。」

===Original===
“I’ve finished my breakfast, Mother.” He rose.

===Chinese===
「我早饭吃完了，妈妈。」他站起身来。

===Original===
The following autumn saw him a student of architecture at Cornell. He worked hard, studied even during his vacations. He would come home to the heat and humidity of the Illinois summers and spend hours each day in his own room that he had fitted up with a long work table and a drawing board. His T-square was at hand; two triangles﻿—a 45 and a 60; his compass; a pair of dividers. Selina sometimes stood behind him watching him as he carefully worked on the tracing paper. His contempt for the local architecture was now complete. Especially did he hold forth on the subject of the apartment-houses that were mushrooming on every street in Chicago from Hyde Park on the south to Evanston on the north. Chicago was very elegant in speaking of these; never called them “flats”; always apartments. In front of each of these (there were usually six to a building), was stuck a little glass-enclosed cubicle known as a sun-parlour. In these (sometimes you heard them spoken of, grandly, as solariums) Chicago dwellers took refuge from the leaden skies, the heavy lake atmosphere, the gray mist and fog and smoke that so frequently swathed the city in gloom. They were done in yellow or rose cretonnes. Silk lamp shades glowed therein, and flower-laden boxes. In these frank little boxes Chicago read its paper, sewed, played bridge, even ate its breakfast. It never pulled down the shades.

===Chinese===
第二年秋天，他进了康奈尔学建筑。他功课拼命，连假期也用功。伊利诺伊的夏天又热又潮，他回了家，每天在自己房里一待几个钟头——屋里备了一张长工作台和一块绘图板。丁字尺在手边；两块三角板——一块 45 度的，一块 60 度的；圆规；一副分规。塞莉娜有时站在他身后，看他小心翼翼地在描图纸上画。他对本地建筑的鄙夷如今臻于完备。他尤其爱就公寓楼大发议论——那种楼在芝加哥条条街上雨后春笋般冒出来，南起城南的海德公园，北到城北的埃文斯顿。芝加哥人说起这些楼来口气十分文雅；从不叫它们「flats（普通楼房套间）」，一律称 apartments（公寓）。每套公寓（一栋楼通常六套）前面，都戳着一个玻璃小隔间，名曰日光廊。芝加哥的住户们躲进这里，躲避铅灰色的天、湖上湿重的空气、还有那常常把全城裹进阴晦里去的灰雾与烟。小间里张的是黄色或玫瑰色的印花装饰布。绸灯罩在里面发亮，还有鲜花满栽的箱子。芝加哥就在这些坦坦荡荡的小玻璃匣子里读报、做针线、打桥牌，甚至吃早饭。窗帘是从来不拉的。

===Original===
“Terrible!” Dirk fumed. “Not only are they hideous in themselves, stuck on the front of those houses like three pairs of spectacles; but the lack of decent privacy! They do everything but bathe in ’em. Have they never heard the advice given people who live in glass houses!”

===Chinese===
「糟透了！」德克忿忿道。「这些玩意儿本身就丑，戳在那些楼正面，活像三副眼镜；更别说毫无体面的私隐了！他们在里面什么都干，就差洗澡了。人家给玻璃房子里的住户下的那句忠告，他们就从来没听说过吗！」

===Original===
By his junior year he was talking in a large way about the Beaux Arts. But Selina did not laugh at this. “Perhaps,” she thought. “Who can tell! After a year or two in an office here, why not another year of study in Paris if he needs it.”

===Chinese===
到了大三，他已经在大谈布扎艺术（Beaux Arts，法国美术学院派）了。塞莉娜听了并不笑。「也许呢，」她想。「谁说得准！在此地的事务所干上一两年，他要需要，何不再去巴黎读上一年。」

===Original===
Though it was her busiest time on the farm Selina went to Ithaca for his graduation in 1913. He was twenty-two and, she was calmly sure, the best-looking man in his class. Undeniably he was a figure to please the eye; tall, well-built, as his father had been, and blond, too, like his father, except for his eyes. These were brown﻿—not so dark as Selina’s, but with some of the soft liquid quality of her glance. They strengthened his face, somehow; gave him an ardent look of which he was not conscious. Women, feeling the ardour of that dark glance turned upon them, were likely to credit him with feelings toward themselves of which he was quite innocent. They did not know that the glance and its effect were mere matters of pigmentation and eye-conformation. Then, too, the gaze of a man who talks little is always more effective than that of one who is loquacious.

===Chinese===
虽说正赶上农场里最忙的时节，1913 年德克毕业，塞莉娜还是去了伊萨卡（Ithaca）。他二十二岁，而且她平平静静地确信，是全班最俊美的男子。无可否认，他是副悦目的身量：高挑，健壮，像他父亲当年，也是一头金发，像他父亲——只是眼睛不随。眼睛是褐色的——不如塞莉娜的黑，却带了几分她那一瞥柔润水灵的神韵。不知怎么，这双眼睛给他的脸添了力量；给了他一副热烈的神情，他自己却浑然不觉。女人们感到那深色的一瞥热辣辣地落在自己身上，往往以为他对她们怀着情意，其实他全然无辜。她们不知道，那一瞥和它的效力，不过是色素和眼形的问题。再说，话少的男人凝视起来，总比话多的男人有力得多。

===Original===
Selina, in her black silk dress, and her plain black hat, and her sensible shoes was rather a quaint little figure amongst all those vivacious, bevoiled, and beribboned mammas. But a distinctive little figure, too. Dirk need not be ashamed of her. She eyed the rather paunchy, prosperous, middle-aged fathers and thought, with a pang, how much handsomer Pervus would have been than any of these, if only he could have lived to see this day. Then, involuntarily, she wondered if this day would ever have occurred, had Pervus lived. Chided herself for thinking thus.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜穿着她的黑绸衣裳、素黑帽子、合脚实惠的鞋子，夹在那些活泼泼、蒙着面纱、缀着缎带的妈妈们中间，颇是一个别致的小小身影。可也是个出众的小身影。德克不必为她害臊。她打量着那些大腹便便、发福体面的中年父亲们，心头一酸，想珀维斯若是活到今日，比这些人里随便哪个都要俊朗得多。随即又不由自主地想：倘若珀维斯活着，这日子还会不会有呢。又怪自己不该这样想。

===Original===
When he returned to Chicago, Dirk went into the office of Hollis & Sprague, Architects. He thought himself lucky to work with this firm, for it was doing much to guide Chicago’s taste in architecture away from the box car. Already Michigan Boulevard’s skyline soared somewhat above the grimly horizontal. But his work there was little more than that of draughtsman, and his weekly stipend could hardly be dignified by the term of salary. But he had large ideas about architecture and he found expression for his suppressed feelings on his weekends spent with Selina at the farm. “Baroque” was the word with which he dismissed the new Beachside Hotel, north. He said the new Lincoln Park bandstand looked like an igloo. He said that the city council ought to order the Potter Palmer mansion destroyed as a blot on the landscape, and waxed profane on the subject of the east face of the Public Library Building, down town.

===Chinese===
回到芝加哥，德克进了霍利斯与斯普拉格建筑师事务所（Hollis & Sprague, Architects）。他自认为进这家事务所是运气，因为这家正在大力把芝加哥的建筑趣味从棚车样式里领出来。密歇根林荫大道的天际线，已经多少超脱了那种阴沉的水平线了。可他在那里干的，跟绘图员差不太多，每礼拜那点津贴，实在当不起「薪水」二字。但他对建筑怀抱大志，周末回农场陪塞莉娜时，就把胸中郁积的块垒尽数倾吐。「巴洛克」一词，是他给北边新开的滨海旅馆（Beachside Hotel）下的判词。他说林肯公园新音乐台活像一座冰屋。他说市议会该下令拆掉波特·帕尔默宅邸，因为那是有损观瞻的一块疮疤；骂起市中心公共图书馆大楼的东立面来，更是口出秽言。

===Original===
“Never mind,” Selina assured him, happily. “It was all thrown up so hastily. Remember that just yesterday, or the day before, Chicago was an Indian fort, with teepees where towers are now, and mud wallows in place of asphalt. Beauty needs time to perfect it. Perhaps we’ve been waiting all these years for just such youngsters as you. And maybe some day I’ll be driving down Michigan Boulevard with a distinguished visitor﻿—Roelf Pool, perhaps. Why not? Let’s say Roelf Pool, the famous sculptor. And he’ll say, ‘Who designed that building﻿—the one that is so strong and yet so light? So gay and graceful, and yet so reticent!’ And I’ll say, ‘Oh, that! That’s one of the earlier efforts of my son, Dirk DeJong.’ ”

===Chinese===
「别急，」塞莉娜快活地安慰他。「这一切都是仓促间堆起来的。别忘了，就在昨天，或者前天，芝加哥还是座印第安人的堡子，如今塔楼林立的地方竖着尖皮帐篷，柏油马路的位子还是烂泥塘。美需要时间来成全。说不定这些年我们等的，正是你们这样的后生呢。也说不定有朝一日，我载着一位贵客驶下密歇根林荫大道——也许是鲁尔夫·普尔。有什么不可以？就说那是鲁尔夫·普尔，鼎鼎大名的雕塑家。他会说：『那座楼是谁设计的——那么厚重，又那么轻灵？那么欢快优雅，又那么矜持！』我就说：『哦，那个呀！那是我儿子德克·德容早期的作品。』」

===Original===
But Dirk pulled at his pipe moodily; shook his head. “Oh, you don’t know, Mother. It’s so damned slow. First thing you know I’ll be thirty. And what am I! An office boy﻿—or little more than that﻿—at Hollis’s.”

===Chinese===
可德克闷闷地抽着烟斗；摇了摇头。「唉，你不知道，妈妈。这一行慢得要死。一转眼我就三十了。到那时我又是个什么！在霍利斯手底下当个办公室杂役——或者比杂役强不了多少。」

===Original===
During his university years Dirk had seen much of the Arnolds, Eugene and Paula, but it sometimes seemed to Selina that he avoided these meetings﻿—these parties and weekends. She was content that this should be so, for she guessed that the matter of money held him back. She thought it was well that he should realize the difference now. Eugene had his own car﻿—one of five in the Arnold garage. Paula, too, had hers. She had been one of the first Chicago girls to drive a gas car; had breezed about Chicago’s boulevards in one when she had been little more than a child in short skirts. At the wheel she was dexterous, daredevil, incredibly relaxed. Her fascination for Dirk was strong. Selina knew that, too. In the last year or two he had talked very little of Paula and that, Selina knew, meant that he was hard hit.

===Chinese===
大学那几年，德克跟阿诺德家——尤金和宝拉——过从甚密，可有的时候塞莉娜觉得他在躲着这些来往——这些宴会和周末。她倒乐于如此，因为她猜是手头紧绊住了他。她觉得他现在就认清这层差别，是好事。尤金有自己的汽车——阿诺德家车库里的五辆之一。宝拉也有她的一辆。她是最早开汽油车的芝加哥姑娘之一；还在穿短裙的小丫头年纪，就已经驾着车在芝加哥的林荫大道上兜风了。握起方向盘，她手疾眼快，天不怕地不怕，松弛得叫人难以置信。她对德克的魅力是强大的。这一点塞莉娜也知道。这一两年他很少提宝拉，而这，塞莉娜明白，意味着他陷得深了。

===Original===
Sometimes Paula and Eugene drove out to the farm, making the distance from their new north-shore house to the DeJong place far south in some breathtaking number of minutes. Eugene would appear in rakish cap, loose London coat, knickers, queer brogans with an English look about them, a carefully careless looseness about the hang and fit of his jacket. Paula did not affect sports clothes for herself. She was not the type, she said. Slim, dark, vivacious, she wore slinky clothes﻿—crêpes, chiffons. Her feet were slim in sheer silk stockings and slippers with buckles. Her eyes were languorous, lovely. She worshipped luxury and said so.

===Chinese===
有时宝拉和尤金开车来农场，从他们北湖滨的新宅到城南老远的德容家庄子，短短几分钟就开到了，快得叫人屏气。尤金到场时，歪戴着俏皮的便帽，宽松的伦敦式外套，灯笼短裤，一双样式古怪、透着英国气的粗革皮鞋，上衣的肥瘦剪裁带着一种精心雕琢出来的随便。宝拉却不爱给自己置运动装。她说自己不是那个类型。她黑瘦窈窕，活泼泼的，穿贴身显线条的衣裳——绉纱、雪纺绸。一双脚匀纤秀气，罩着薄如蝉翼的丝袜，登着带扣襻的便鞋。她的眼睛慵倦而可爱。她崇拜奢侈，而且直言不讳。

===Original===
“I’ll have to marry money,” she declared. “Now that they’ve finished calling poor Grandpa a beef-baron and taken I don’t know how many millions away from him, we’re practically on the streets.”

===Chinese===
「我只好嫁个有钱人，」她宣布道。「如今他们把可怜的外公牛肉大王也叫够了，又不知从他身上弄走了几千几百万，我们差不多算流落街头了。」

===Original===
“You look it!” from Dirk; and there was bitterness beneath his light tone.

===Chinese===
「瞧你这流落街头的样儿！」德克说；轻快的口气底下埋着苦涩。

===Original===
“Well, it’s true. All this silly muckraking in the past ten years or more. Poor Father! Of course Granddad was pur-ty rough, let me tell you. I read some of the accounts of that last indictment﻿—the 1910 one﻿—and I must say I gathered that dear old Aug made Jesse James look like a philanthropist. I should think, at his age, he’d be a little scared. After all, when you’re over seventy you’re likely to have some doubts and fears about punishment in the next world. But not a grand old pirate like Grandfather. He’ll sack and burn and plunder until he goes down with the ship. And it looks to me as if the old boat had a pretty strong list to starboard right now. Father says himself that unless a war breaks, or something, which isn’t at all likely, the packing industry is going to spring a leak.”

===Chinese===
「唉，这是实情。这十来年净是这种无聊的揭黑闹剧。可怜的爸爸！当然了，外公他老人家是糙得很哪，我告诉你。那次起诉——1910 年那回——的案卷我读过一些，我得说，据我领会，亲爱的老奥格把杰西·詹姆斯（Jesse James）衬得都像个慈善家了。要我是他那个年纪，多少会有点心惊胆战。毕竟人过了七十，难免对来世的报应存几分疑惧。可外公那样堂堂的老海盗才不呢。他要一直烧杀抢掠下去，直到跟船一起沉没。依我看，这会儿那条老船已经朝右舷歪得够呛了。爸爸自己都说，除非打起仗来，或者出点什么大事——这可半点指望不上——肉品加工业就要漏水了。」

===Original===
“Elaborate figure of speech,” murmured Eugene. The four of them﻿—Paula, Dirk, Eugene, and Selina﻿—, were sitting on the wide screened porch that Selina had had built at the southwest corner of the house. Paula was, of course, in the couch-swing. Occasionally she touched one slim languid foot to the floor and gave indolent impetus to the couch.

===Chinese===
「好一个繁复的修辞，」尤金咕哝道。他们四个——宝拉、德克、尤金和塞莉娜——坐在房子西南角那道宽敞的纱廊上，那是塞莉娜叫人修的。宝拉当然窝在秋千吊椅里。偶尔，她用一只纤细慵懒的脚尖点一点地，给吊椅添一点懒洋洋的推力。

===Original===
“It is, rather, isn’t it? Might as well finish it, then. Darling Aug’s been the grand old captain right through the vi’age. Dad’s never been more than a pretty bum second mate. And as for you, Gene my love, cabin boy would be, y’understand me, big.” Eugene had gone into the business a year before.

===Chinese===
「可不是挺繁复嘛。那就索性繁复到底吧。亲爱的奥格老外公，整趟航程里都是那位威风凛凛的老船长。爸爸呢，从来不过是个蹩脚透顶的大副。至于你嘛，我的宝贝吉恩，你呀，听我说，能当上舱面小听差就算高升啦。」尤金一年前进了这一行。

===Original===
“What can you expect,” retorted Eugene, “of a lad that hates salt pork? And every other kind of pig meat?” He despised the yards and all that went with it.

===Chinese===
「你能指望什么呢，」尤金反唇相讥，「从一个小伙子身上——他连咸猪肉都恨？还连带恨着其他各式各样的猪肉？」他鄙夷场子和跟场子沾边的一切。

===Original===
Selina now got up and walked to the end of the porch. She looked out across the fields, shading her eyes with her hand. “There’s Adam coming in with the last load for the day. He’ll be driving into town now. Cornelius started an hour ago.” The DeJong farm sent two great loads to the city now. Selina was contemplating the purchase of one of the large automobile trucks that would do away with the plodding horses and save hours of time on the trip. She went down the steps now on her way to oversee the loading of Adam Bras’s wagon. At the bottom of the steps she turned. “Why can’t you two stay to supper? You can quarrel comfortably right through the meal and drive home in the cool of the evening.”

===Chinese===
塞莉娜起身走到廊子尽头。她手搭凉棚，朝田里望去。「亚当拉着今天最后一车回来了。他这就要赶进城了。科尼利厄斯一个钟头前就动身了。」如今德容家农场每天往城里发两大车菜。塞莉娜正盘算着买一辆大汽车卡车，好废掉那些慢吞腾的马，来回省下好几个钟头。她走下台阶，去督着装亚当·布拉斯的大车。走到台阶底下她回过头。「你们俩何不留下吃晚饭？正好一路舒舒服服吵着吃完这顿饭，等傍晚凉快了再开车回去。」

===Original===
“I’ll stay,” said Paula, “thanks. If you’ll have all kinds of vegetables, cooked and uncooked. The cooked ones smothered in cream and oozing butter. And let me go out into the fields and pick ’em myself like Maud Muller or Marie Antoinette or any of those make-believe rustic gals.”

===Chinese===
「我留下，」宝拉说，「谢谢。只要各样蔬菜都来点儿，做熟的和生吃的都要。熟的呢，要奶油焖得浓浓的、黄油汪着往外渗。还得让我自己下地去采，像莫德·马勒（Maud Muller），或者玛丽·安托瓦内特（Marie Antoinette），或者随便哪个装模作样的牧歌姑娘那样。」

===Original===
In her French-heeled slippers and her filmy silk stockings she went out into the rich black furrows of the fields, Dirk carrying the basket.

===Chinese===
她踩着法式高跟便鞋、罩着薄纱丝袜，走进田里油黑的垄沟，德克替她提着篮子。

===Original===
“Asparagus,” she ordered first. Then, “But where is it? Is *that* it!”

===Chinese===
「芦笋，」她头一个点的是这个。接着，「可它在哪儿呢？*那*就是芦笋？」

===Original===
“You dig for it, idiot,” said Dirk, stooping, and taking from his basket the queerly curved sharp knife or spud used for cutting the asparagus shoots. “Cut the shoots three or four inches below the surface.”

===Chinese===
「芦笋是往土里抠的，傻瓜，」德克说着弯下腰，从篮子里取出那把弯头怪样的小快刀，也就是铲刀，专用来割芦笋嫩茎的。「要在土面底下三四英寸的地方下刀割。」

===Original===
“Oh, let me do it!” She was down on her silken knees in the dirt, ruined a goodly patch of the fine tender shoots, gave it up and sat watching Dirk’s expert manipulation of the knife. “Let’s have radishes, and corn, and tomatoes and lettuce and peas and artichokes and﻿—”

===Chinese===
「哎，让我来！」她那双裹着丝袜的膝盖跪进泥里，毁掉好一片细嫩的芦笋尖，只好作罢，坐在一边看德克熟练地使刀。「咱们要小萝卜、玉米、番茄、生菜、豌豆、洋蓟，还有——」

===Original===
“Artichokes grow in California, not Illinois.” He was more than usually uncommunicative, and noticeably moody.

===Chinese===
「洋蓟长在加利福尼亚，不长在伊利诺伊。」他比平时更不爱说话，闷闷的，一望而知。

===Original===
Paula remarked it. “Why the Othello brow?”

===Chinese===
宝拉注意到了。「干嘛摆出这副奥赛罗的眉头？」

===Original===
“You didn’t mean that rot, did you? about marrying a rich man.”

===Chinese===
「你刚才那些混话不是当真的吧？关于嫁个有钱人的。」

===Original===
“Of course I meant it. What other sort of man do you think I ought to marry?” He looked at her, silently. She smiled. “Yes, wouldn’t I make an ideal bride for a farmer!”

===Chinese===
「当然是当真的。不然你以为我该嫁什么样的人？」他默默看着她。她笑了。「是啊，给一个农夫当新娘，我不正理想吗！」

===Original===
“I’m not a farmer.”

===Chinese===
「我不是农夫。」

===Original===
“Well, architect then. Your job as draughtsman at Hollis & Sprague’s must pay you all of twenty-five a week.”

===Chinese===
「好吧，那就建筑师。你在霍利斯与斯普拉格当绘图员，周薪想必足足有二十五块吧。」

===Original===
“Thirty-five,” said Dirk, grimly. “What’s that got to do with it!”

===Chinese===
「三十五，」德克咬着牙说。「那跟这事有什么相干！」

===Original===
“Not a thing, darling.” She stuck out one foot. “These slippers cost thirty.”

===Chinese===
「半点相干都没有，亲爱的。」她伸出一只脚。「这双鞋就花了三十块。」

===Original===
“I won’t be getting thirty-five a week all my life. You’ve got brains enough to know that. Eugene wouldn’t be getting that much if he weren’t the son of his father.”

===Chinese===
「我不会一辈子都挣三十五块周薪。这个你脑子够用，明白。尤金要不是他爸爸的儿子，连这个数也拿不上。」

===Original===
“The grandson of his grandfather,” Paula corrected him. “And I’m not so sure he wouldn’t. Gene’s a born mechanic if they’d just let him work at it. He’s crazy about engines and all that junk. But no﻿—‘Millionaire Packer’s Son Learns Business from Bottom Rung of Ladder.’ Picture of Gene in workman’s overalls and cap in the Sunday papers. He drives to the office on Michigan at ten and leaves at four and he doesn’t know a steer from a cow when he sees it.”

===Chinese===
「是他外公的外孙，」宝拉纠正他。「而且他拿不拿得上，我还不一定呢。吉恩天生是块当机械匠的料，只要他们肯让他干。他对引擎还有那些破烂玩艺儿迷得要死。可是不行——『百万富翁肉品商之子自梯子最底一级学起』。礼拜天的报纸上登着吉恩穿工装裤、戴布帽子的照片。他十点钟开车上密歇根大道的写字间，四点钟走人，见了一头牛也分不出公母。」

===Original===
“I don’t care a damn about Gene. I’m talking about you. You were joking, weren’t you?”

===Chinese===
「吉恩关我什么事。我说的是你。你方才是开玩笑，对不对？」

===Original===
“I wasn’t. I’d hate being poor, or even just moderately rich. I’m used to money﻿—loads of it. I’m twenty-four. And I’m looking around.”

===Chinese===
「不是开玩笑。穷我受不了，就连不穷不富的中等日子我也受不了。我过惯了钱——大把的钱。我二十四了。正在物色呢。」

===Original===
He kicked an innocent beet-top with his boot. “You like me better than any man you know.”

===Chinese===
他用靴子踢了一脚无辜的甜菜缨子。「你对我比对你认识的任何男人都喜欢。」

===Original===
“Of course I do. Just my luck.”

===Chinese===
「当然喜欢。可不就数我这运气。」

===Original===
“Well, then!”

===Chinese===
「那么，这不结了！」

===Original===
“Well, then, let’s take these weggibles in and have ’em cooked in cream, as ordered.”

===Chinese===
「那么，这不结了——咱们把这篮菜菜儿（weggibles）提进去，照吩咐的拿奶油煮了吃。」

===Original===
She made a pretense of lifting the heavy basket. Dirk snatched it roughly out of her hand so that she gave a little cry and looked ruefully down at the red mark on her palm. He caught her by the shoulder﻿—even shook her a little. “Look here, Paula. Do you mean to tell me you’d marry a man simply because he happened to have a lot of money!”

===Chinese===
她装模作样去提那只沉甸甸的篮子。德克一把从她手里夺过去，夺得很重，她低低叫了一声，懊恼地看着掌心上的红印子。他抓住她的肩膀——甚至摇了她几下。「你听着，宝拉。你是要告诉我，一个男人单单因为碰巧有一大笔钱，你就会嫁给他吗！」

===Original===
“Perhaps not simply because he had a lot of money. But it certainly would be a factor, among other things. Certainly he would be preferable to a man who knocked me about the fields as if I were a bag of potatoes.”

===Chinese===
「也许不光是因为他有一大笔钱。可它肯定算得上一条，跟别的条件摆在一块儿。总归比一个把我当一袋土豆似的在田里推来搡去的男人可取吧。」

===Original===
“Oh, forgive me. But﻿—listen, Paula﻿—you know I’m﻿—gosh!﻿—And there I am stuck in an architect’s office and it’ll be years before I﻿—”

===Chinese===
「哦，原谅我。可——你听着，宝拉——你知道我——天哪！——而我偏偏窝在一家建筑师事务所里，还得熬上多少年我才能——」

===Original===
“Yes, but it’ll probably be years before I meet the millions I require, too. So why bother? And even if I do, you and I can be just as good friends.”

===Chinese===
「是啊，可我碰上我需要的那几百万，多半也得些年头呢。所以急什么呢？就算碰上了，你我照旧做好朋友，不也一样。」

===Original===
“Oh, shut up. Don’t pull that ingénue stuff on me, please. Remember I’ve known you since you were ten years old.”

===Chinese===
「哦，闭嘴。求你别跟我来那套天真小儿女（ingénue）的把戏。别忘了你十岁上我就认识你了。」

===Original===
“And you know just how black my heart is, don’t you, what? You want, really, some nice hearty lass who can tell asparagus from peas when she sees ’em, and who’ll offer to race you from here to the kitchen.”

===Chinese===
「你也明明知道我这颗心有多黑，对不对，嗯？你真正想要的，是哪个结实爽朗的好姑娘，见了芦笋豌豆分得清，还会主动提出从这里跟你赛跑到厨房。」

===Original===
“God forbid!”

===Chinese===
「老天爷可别！」

===Original===
Six months later Paula Arnold was married to Theodore A. Storm, a man of fifty, a friend of her father’s, head of so many companies, stockholder in so many banks, director of so many corporations that even old Aug Hempel seemed a recluse from business in comparison. She never called him Teddy. No one ever did. Theodore Storm was a large man﻿—not exactly stout, perhaps, but flabby. His inches saved him from grossness. He had a large white serious face, fine thick dark hair, graying at the temples, and he dressed very well except for a leaning toward rather effeminate ties. He built for Paula a town house on the Lake Shore drive in the region known as the Gold Coast. The house looked like a restrained public library. There was a country place beyond Lake Forest far out on the north shore, sloping down to the lake and surrounded by acres and acres of fine woodland, expertly parked. There were drives, ravines, brooks, bridges, hothouses, stables, a racetrack, gardens, dairies, fountains, bosky paths, keeper’s cottage (twice the size of Selina’s farmhouse). Within three years Paula had two children, a boy and a girl. “There! That’s done,” she said. Her marriage was a great mistake and she knew it. For the war, coming in 1914, a few months after her wedding, sent the Hempel-Arnold interests skyrocketing. Millions of pounds of American beef and pork were shipped to Europe. In two years the Hempel fortune was greater than it ever had been. Paula was up to her eyes in relief work for Bleeding Belgium. All the Gold Coast was. The Beautiful Mrs. Theodore A. Storm in her Gift Shop Conducted for the Relief of Bleeding Belgium.

===Chinese===
六个月后，宝拉·阿诺德嫁给了西奥多·A·斯托姆（Theodore A. Storm）。此人五十岁，是她父亲的朋友，身兼太多太多家公司的老板、太多太多家银行的股东、太多太多家企业的董事，相形之下，连老奥格·亨佩尔都算得上生意场上的隐士了。她从不叫他泰迪。谁也从不叫。西奥多·斯托姆是个大块头的男人——也许说不上肥胖，但松松垮垮。全凭身量高大，他才免于臃肿蠢笨。他有一张白皙、庄重的大脸，一头浓密的深色好头发，两鬓染霜，衣着极为考究，只是偏爱相当女人气的领带。他为宝拉在湖滨大道（Lake Shore Drive）俗称黄金海岸（Gold Coast）的地段盖了一所城里宅邸。那房子看上去像一座收敛着不事张扬的公共图书馆。另有一处乡下产业，在莱克福里斯特（Lake Forest）以外、北湖滨的极远处，坡地逶迤而下直抵湖边，四围是成百英亩的好林地，园艺打理得一丝不苟。有林荫车道，有沟谷，有溪涧，有小桥，有暖花房，有马厩，有一条跑马道，有花园，有奶牛场，有喷泉，有绿荫小径，有守园人小屋（足有塞莉娜家庄子的两倍大）。三年之内，宝拉添了两个孩子，一男一女。「好啦！这件事办完了，」她说。她的婚姻是个大错，她自己心知肚明。因为 1914 年大战骤至，就在她婚后几个月，把亨佩尔－阿诺德两家的产业送上了天。几百万磅的美国牛肉猪肉运往欧洲。两年之内，亨佩尔家的家产超过了以往任何时候。宝拉一头扎进救助「流血的比利时」（Bleeding Belgium）的赈济工作，深没及眼。整个黄金海岸莫不如此。美丽的西奥多·A·斯托姆夫人，坐镇她的「为救济流血的比利时而设的礼品店」。

===Original===
Dirk had not seen her in months. She telephoned him unexpectedly one Friday afternoon in his office at Hollis & Sprague’s.

===Chinese===
德克有好几个月没见到她了。一个星期五下午，她出其不意地把电话打到他在霍利斯与斯普拉格的办公室。

===Original===
“Come out and spend Saturday and Sunday with us, won’t you? We’re running away to the country this afternoon. I’m so sick of Bleeding Belgium, you can’t imagine. I’m sending the children out this morning. I can’t get away so early. I’ll call for you in the roadster this afternoon at four and drive you out myself.”

===Chinese===
「到乡下来跟我们一起过星期六星期天，好不好？我们今天下午就逃下乡去。流血的比利时，我都腻烦透了，你想不到有多腻。孩子们今天上午先打发过去。我没办法那么早脱身。下午四点我开双座跑车来接你，亲自送你出城。」

===Original===
“I am going to spend the weekend with Mother. She’s expecting me.”

===Chinese===
「这个周末我要陪妈妈过。她等着我呢。」

===Original===
“Bring her along.”

===Chinese===
「把她也带来嘛。」

===Original===
“She wouldn’t come. You know she doesn’t enjoy all that velvet-footed servitor stuff.”

===Chinese===
「她不会来的。你知道，那一套仆役蹑着天鹅绒脚步伺候人的排场，她不待见。」

===Original===
“Oh, but we live quite simply out there, really. Just sort of rough it. Do come, Dirk. I’ve got some plans to talk over with you﻿ ﻿… How’s the job?”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，我们在乡下住得可简朴了，真的。简直算是苦熬呢。一定来呀，德克。我有几桩打算要跟你商量……工作怎么样？」

===Original===
“Oh, good enough. There’s very little building going on, you know.”

===Chinese===
「哦，还过得去。眼下载不了多少房子，你知道的。」

===Original===
“Will you come?”

===Chinese===
「你来不来？」

===Original===
“I don’t think I﻿—”

===Chinese===
「我看我不——」

===Original===
“I’ll call for you at four. I’ll be at the curb. Don’t keep me waiting, will you? The cops fuss so if you park in the Loop after four.”

===Chinese===
「四点我去接你。我停在路边。别叫我干等，好不好？四点以后你若在卢普区（the Loop）泊车，警察就要啰唆个没完。」

## XV / 第十五章

===Original===
“Run along!” said Selina, when he called her on the farm telephone. “It’ll do you good. You’ve been as grumpy as a gander for weeks. How about shirts? And you left one pair of flannel tennis pants out here last fall﻿—clean ones. Won’t you need﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「快去吧！」他打电话到农场来时，塞莉娜说。「去了对你有好处。你这几个星期火气大得像只公鹅。衬衫怎么办？还有，去年秋天你有一条法兰绒网球裤落在这儿——倒是干净的。你不需要……

===Original===
In town he lived in a large front room and alcove on the third floor of a handsome old-fashioned three-story-and-basement house in Deming Place. He used the front room as a living room, the alcove as a bedroom. He and Selina had furnished it together, discarding all of the room’s original belongings except the bed, a table, and one fat comfortable faded old armchair whose brocade surface hinted a past grandeur. When he had got his books ranged in open shelves along one wall, soft-shaded lamps on table and desk, the place looked more than livable; lived in. During the process of furnishing Selina got into the way of coming into town for a day or two to prowl the auction rooms and the secondhand stores. She had a genius for this sort of thing; hated the spick-and-span varnish and veneer of the new furniture to be got in the regular way.

===Chinese===
在城里，他住在迪明广场（Deming Place）一幢漂亮的老式三层带地下室的宅子里，占了三楼一间大前房连一间凹室。前房用作起居室，凹室当卧室。这屋子是他和塞莉娜一起布置的，屋里原有的家什一律撤去，只留下一张床、一张桌子和一把肥墩墩、软和和、褪了色的旧扶手椅——椅面的锦缎还隐约透着几分当年的气派。等他把书一排排码上沿墙的敞开式书架，桌上、书桌上摆好罩着浅色灯罩的台灯，这地方看上去不止是可以住人，简直是住出了人家的味道。布置期间，塞莉娜养成了习惯，时不时进城一两天，去拍卖行和旧货店里转悠寻宝。她干这个是天生的行家；最恨正经渠道买来的新家具那股锃光瓦亮的清漆和贴面劲儿。

===Original===
“Any piece of furniture, I don’t care how beautiful it is, has got to be lived with, and kicked about, and rubbed down, and mistreated by servants, and repolished, and knocked around and dusted and sat on or slept in or eaten off of before it develops its real character,” Selina said. “A good deal like human beings. I’d rather have my old maple table, mellow with age and rubbing, that Pervus’s father put together himself by hand seventy years ago, than all the mahogany library slabs on Wabash Avenue.”

===Chinese===
「一件家具，我不管它多漂亮，总得先让人朝夕相处地用着，被踢来踢去，蹭出光泽，被佣人糟蹋，重新上光，磕了碰了，掸了灰，在上头坐过、睡过、吃过饭，才养得出真正的品性。」塞莉娜说。「跟人很有点像。瓦巴什大道上全部的红木大书桌，也抵不上我那张老枫木桌——珀维斯的父亲七十年前亲手做的，让岁月和摩挲养出了一层温润。」

===Original===
She enjoyed these rare trips into town; made a holiday of them. Dirk would take her to the theatre and she would sit entranced. Her feeling for this form of entertainment was as fresh and eager as it had been in the days of the Daly Stock Company when she, a little girl, had been seated in the parquet with her father, Simeon Peake. Strangely enough, considering the lack of what the world calls romance and adventure in her life, she did not like the motion pictures. “All the difference in the world,” she would say, “between the movies and the thrill I get out of a play at the theatre. My, yes! Like fooling with paper dolls when you could be playing with a real live baby.”

===Chinese===
这种难得的进城之游她乐在其中，简直当成过节。德克带她上剧院，她坐在那儿如痴如醉。她对这种娱乐的感情，跟戴利轮演剧团（Daly Stock Company）的年代一样新鲜热切——那时她还是个小姑娘，由父亲西缅·皮克带着坐在正厅前排。说来奇怪，她这辈子尽管少有世人所谓的浪漫与奇遇，她却不喜欢电影。「电影，跟我在剧院里看一出好戏时浑身的战栗，」她总说，「是天差地远的两回事。可不是嘛！就像放着活蹦乱跳的真娃娃不抱，偏去摆弄纸娃娃。」

===Original===
She developed a mania for nosing into strange corners of the huge sprawling city; seemed to discover a fresh wonder on each visit. In a short time she was more familiar with Chicago than was Dirk﻿—for that matter, than old Aug Hempel who had lived in it for over half a century but who never had gone far afield in his pendulum path between the yards and his house, his house and the yards.

===Chinese===
她迷上了钻这座四下铺展的庞然大城里的生僻角落；似乎每来一趟都能发现一桩新的奇观。没过多久，她比德克还熟芝加哥——认真说起来，比老奥格·亨佩尔还熟；老奥格在这城里住了半个多世纪，却从没走出过场子到家、家到场子那条钟摆路线多远。

===Original===
The things that excited her about Chicago did not seem to interest Dirk at all. Sometimes she took a vacant room for a day or two in Dirk’s boarding house. “What do you think!” she would say to him, breathlessly, when he returned from the office in the evening. “I’ve been way over on the northwest side. It’s another world. It’s﻿—it’s Poland. Cathedrals and shops and men sitting in restaurants all day long reading papers and drinking coffee and playing dominoes or something like it. And what do you think I found out! Chicago’s got the second largest Polish population of any city in the world. In the *world*!”

===Chinese===
芝加哥这些让她兴奋的东西，德克似乎毫无兴趣。有时她就在德克住的包伙公寓里开个空房住上一两天。「你猜怎么着！」傍晚他从事务所回来，她会气喘吁吁地对他说。「我今天跑到城西北那一头去了。那是另一个世界。那儿是——是波兰。大教堂啊，铺子啊，男人们成天坐在饭馆里看报、喝咖啡、玩多米诺骨牌什么的。还有你猜我打听到了什么！全世界数城市，芝加哥的波兰人人口第二多。在*全*世界！」

===Original===
“Yeh?” Dirk would reply, absently.

===Chinese===
「是吗？」德克会心不在焉地应一声。

===Original===
There was nothing absentminded about his tone this afternoon as he talked to his mother on the telephone. “Sure you don’t mind? Then I’ll be out next Saturday. Or I may run out in the middle of the week to stay over night﻿ ﻿… Are you all right?”

===Chinese===
这天下午他在电话里跟母亲说话，语气可一点不含糊。「你真不介意？那我下星期六过去。要不周中间我也跑来住一晚……你身子骨还好吧？」

===Original===
“I’m fine. Be sure and remember all about Paula’s new house so’s you can tell me about it. Julie says it’s like the kind you read of in the novels. She says old Aug saw it just once and now won’t go near it even to visit his grandchildren.”

===Chinese===
「我好着呢。宝拉的新房子你务必看仔细了，回来好讲给我听。朱莉说那房子就跟小说里写的那种一样。她说老奥格只去看过一回，如今连去瞧孙辈都不肯挨近那个门了。」

===Original===
The day was marvellously mild for March in Chicago. Spring, usually so coy in this region, had flung herself at them head first. As the massive revolving door of Dirk’s office building fanned him into the street he saw Paula in her long low sporting roadster at the curb. She was dressed in black. All feminine fashionable and middle-class Chicago was dressed in black. All feminine fashionable and middle-class America was dressed in black. Two years of war had robbed Paris of its husbands, brothers, sons. All Paris walked in black. America, untouched, gayly borrowed the smart habiliments of mourning and now Michigan Boulevard and Fifth Avenue walked demurely in the gloom of crêpe and chiffon; black hats, black gloves, black slippers. Only black was “good” this year.

===Chinese===
就芝加哥的三月来说，这天暖和得出奇。素来在这地方扭扭捏捏的春天，这回一头就扎进了人怀里。德克事务所大楼那扇厚重的旋转门把他扇到街上，他看见宝拉坐在路边那辆长长的、低低的运动型双座跑车里。她一身黑。芝加哥全城的时髦和中产的女性都一身黑。全美国的时髦和中产的女性都一身黑。两年的战争夺走了巴黎的丈夫、兄弟、儿子。整个巴黎穿黑而行。美国毫发无损，欢欢喜喜地借来了时新的丧服行头，于是如今密歇根林荫大道和第五大道（Fifth Avenue）都端庄地行走在绉纱与雪纺的幽暗里：黑帽子，黑手套，黑便鞋。今年只有黑色才算「时兴」。

===Original===
Paula did not wear black well. She was a shade too sallow for these sombre swathings even though relieved by a pearl strand of exquisite colour, flawlessly matched; and a new sly face-powder. Paula smiled up at him, patted the leather seat beside her with one hand that was absurdly thick-fingered in its fur-lined glove.

===Chinese===
宝拉穿黑色不好看。这些暗沉沉的裹缠，她那微显蜡黄的肤色撑不起来，尽管有一串色泽精绝、颗颗无瑕的珍珠项链调剂，又扑了一种新出的、暗地里讨巧的香粉。宝拉仰起脸朝他一笑，一只手拍拍身边的皮座——那只手在毛皮衬里的手套里，手指粗得叫人没法当真。

===Original===
“It’s cold driving. Button up tight. Where’ll we stop for your bag? Are you still in Deming Place?”

===Chinese===
「开着车冷。扣严实点。你的行李袋上哪儿捎？你还住迪明广场吗？」

===Original===
He was still in Deming Place. He climbed into the seat beside her﻿—a feat for the young and nimble. Theodore Storm never tried to double his bulk into the jackknife position necessary to riding in his wife’s roadster. The car was built for speed, not comfort. One sat flat with the length of one’s legs stretched out. Paula’s feet, pedalling brake and clutch so expertly, were inadequately clothed in sheer black silk stockings and slim buckled patent-leather slippers.

===Chinese===
他还住迪明广场。他爬进她身边的座位——这可是年轻、腿脚利索的人才办得到的绝技。西奥多·斯托姆从来不去试把自己那一副大块头对折成折刀，塞进他妻子这辆跑车所必需的那种坐姿。这车为速度而造，不为舒适。人平平地坐着，两条腿一路伸直。宝拉的脚踩着刹车和离合器，熟极了，脚上却只罩着薄薄的黑丝袜和窄小的系带漆皮便鞋，未免穿得太单薄。

===Original===
“You’re not dressed warmly enough,” her husband would have said. “Those shoes are idiotic for driving.” And he would have been right.

===Chinese===
「你穿得不够暖，」她丈夫本该这么说。「穿这鞋开车简直是胡闹。」而且他本该是占理的。

===Original===
Dirk said nothing.

===Chinese===
德克什么也没说。

===Original===
Her manipulation of the wheel was witchcraft. The roadster slid in and out of traffic like a fluid thing, an enamel stream, silent as a swift current in a river. “Can’t let her out here,” said Paula. “Wait till we get past Lincoln Park. Do you suppose they’ll ever really get rid of this terrible Rush Street bridge?” When his house was reached, “I’m coming up,” she said. “I suppose you haven’t any tea?”

===Chinese===
她打方向盘的手法简直是巫术。跑车在车流里滑进滑出，像一件流动的东西，一道珐琅的溪流，悄无声息，像河里一股湍流。「这儿还撒不开她，」宝拉说。「等过了林肯公园再说。你说他们到底会不会真把拉什街（Rush Street）那座讨厌的桥给拆了？」到了他的住处：「我上去了，」她说。「你这儿大概连茶都没有吧？」

===Original===
“Gosh, no! What do you think I am! A young man in an English novel!”

===Chinese===
「天哪，才没有！你把我当成什么人了！英国小说里的小青年吗！」

===Original===
“Now, don’t be provincial and Chicago-ish, Dirk.” They climbed the three flights of stairs. She looked about. Her glance was not disapproving. “This isn’t so bad. Who did it? She did! Very nice. But of course you ought to have your own smart little apartment, with a Jap to do you up. To do that for you, for example.”

===Chinese===
「哎，别这么乡气，别一嘴芝加哥味儿，德克。」他们爬上三段楼梯。她四下看了看。目光里并没有嫌弃的意思。「不算太糟。谁布置的？是她吧！很不错。不过你当然该有一套自己时髦的小公寓，雇个日本男仆替你拾掇。比如说，就替你干这个。」

===Original===
“Yes,” grimly. He was packing his bag﻿—not throwing clothes into it, but folding them deftly, neatly, as the son of a wise mother packs. “My salary’d just about keep him in white linen housecoats.”

===Chinese===
「是啊。」他绷着脸说。他正收拾行李袋——不是把衣裳往里一塞，而是叠得灵巧齐整，一位聪明母亲的儿子收拾行李就是这个架势。「我那点薪水，也就刚够供他穿白色亚麻家居长袍。」

===Original===
She was walking about the living room, picking up a book, putting it down, fingering an ash tray, gazing out of the window, examining a photograph, smoking a cigarette from the box on his table. Restless, nervously alive, catlike. “I’m going to send you some things for your room, Dirk.”

===Chinese===
她在起居室里走来走去，拿起一本书，放下，摸摸烟灰缸，望望窗外，端详一张照片，从他桌上的烟盒里抽支烟点上。躁动不宁，神经质地鲜活，像只猫。「我要给你这屋子送几样东西来，德克。」

===Original===
“For God’s sake don’t!”

===Chinese===
「看在上帝分上，千万别！」

===Original===
“Why not?”

===Chinese===
「为什么不行？」

===Original===
“Two kinds of women in the world. I learned that at college. Those who send men things for their rooms and those that don’t.”

===Chinese===
「世上的女人分两种。这是我在大学里学到的。一种是往男人屋里送东西的，一种是不送的。」

===Original===
“You’re very rude.”

===Chinese===
「你太没礼貌了。」

===Original===
“You asked me. There! I’m all set.” He snapped the lock of his bag. “I’m sorry I can’t give you anything. I haven’t a thing. Not even a glass of wine and a﻿—what is it they say in books?﻿—oh, yeh﻿—a biscuit.”

===Chinese===
「是你自己问的。好了！我收拾停当了。」他啪的一声扣上行李袋的锁。「对不起，我拿不出东西招待你。我一无所有。连一杯葡萄酒和一块——书上是怎么说的来着？——哦，对——一块饼干都拿不出。」

===Original===
In the roadster again they slid smoothly out along the drive, along Sheridan Road, swung sharply around the cemetery curve into Evanston, past the smug middle-class suburban neatness of Wilmette and Winnetka. She negotiated expertly the nerve-racking curves of the Hubbard Woods hills, then maintained a fierce and steady speed for the remainder of the drive.

===Chinese===
重新坐进跑车，他们顺着湖滨大道滑出去，上了谢里登路（Sheridan Road），绕着墓园的弯道一拐进了埃文斯顿，掠过威尔米特（Wilmette）和温内特卡（Winnetka）那种自满的中产阶级郊区的整整洁洁。她熟练地对付了哈伯德伍兹（Hubbard Woods）丘陵那段磨人神经的弯道，随后剩下的路程一直保持着凶猛而平稳的速度。

===Original===
“We call the place Stormwood,” Paula told him. “And nobody outside the dear family knows how fitting that is. Don’t scowl. I’m not going to tell you my marital woes. And don’t you say I asked for it.﻿ ﻿… How’s the job?”

===Chinese===
「我们管这地方叫斯托姆伍德（Stormwood），」宝拉告诉他。「除了亲爱的自家人，谁也不知道这名字起得多贴切。别皱眉。我不打算跟你诉婚姻的苦水。你也别说是我自找的。……差事怎么样？」

===Original===
“Rotten.”

===Chinese===
「糟透了。」

===Original===
“You don’t like it? The work?”

===Chinese===
「你不喜欢？这活儿？」

===Original===
“I like it well enough, only﻿—well, you see we leave the university architectural course thinking we’re all going to be Stanford Whites or Cass Gilberts, tossing off a Woolworth building and making ourselves famous overnight. I’ve spent all yesterday and today planning how to work in space for toilets on every floor of the new office building, six stories high and shaped like a drygoods box, that’s going up on the corner of Milwaukee Avenue and Ashland, west.”

===Chinese===
「活儿我倒挺喜欢，只是——唉，你是知道的，我们从大学建筑系出来的时候，个个以为自己会成为斯坦福·怀特（Stanford White）或者卡斯·吉尔伯特（Cass Gilbert），随手甩出一座伍尔沃思大楼（Woolworth Building），一夜成名。我昨天一整天加今天一整天，都在盘算怎么在新办公楼的每一层腾出厕所的地方——那楼六层高，方方正正像个布匹杂货箱，正在西边密尔沃基大道（Milwaukee Avenue）和阿什兰（Ashland）的街角上往上盖。」

===Original===
“And ten years from now?”

===Chinese===
「那十年以后呢？」

===Original===
“Ten years from now maybe they’ll let me do the plans for the drygoods box all alone.”

===Chinese===
「十年以后，没准儿他们肯放手让我一个人画那个布匹杂货箱的图纸了。」

===Original===
“Why don’t you drop it?”

===Chinese===
「你干吗不撂了它？」

===Original===
He was startled. “Drop it! How do you mean?”

===Chinese===
他吃了一惊。「撂了它！你是什么意思？」

===Original===
“Chuck it. Do something that will bring you quick results. This isn’t an age of waiting. Suppose, twenty years from now, you do plan a grand Gothic office building to grace this new and glorified Michigan Boulevard they’re always shouting about! You’ll be a middle-aged man living in a middle-class house in a middle-class suburb with a middle-class wife.”

===Chinese===
「撂下呗。干点快见效的事。这可不是个等的年头。就算二十年后，你真设计出一座堂皇的哥特式办公楼，给他们成天嚷嚷的那条焕然生辉的密歇根林荫大道增光！你也不过是个中年汉子，住着中产阶级郊区的中产阶级房子，配上一个中产阶级老婆。」

===Original===
“Maybe”﻿—slightly nettled. “And maybe I’ll be the Sir Christopher Wren of Chicago.”

===Chinese===
「也许吧」——他有点着恼。「也许我会成为芝加哥的克里斯托弗·雷恩爵士（Sir Christopher Wren）呢。」

===Original===
“Who’s he?”

===Chinese===
「他是谁？」

===Original===
“Good G﻿—﻿—, how often have you been in London?”

===Chinese===
「老天爷啊——你去过几回伦敦？」

===Original===
“Three times.”

===Chinese===
「三回。」

===Original===
“Next time you find yourself there you might cast your eye over a very nice little structure called St. Paul’s Cathedral. I’ve never seen it but it has been very well spoken of.”

===Chinese===
「下回你到了那儿，不妨去看看一座很不错的小建筑，名叫圣保罗大教堂（St. Paul’s Cathedral）。我没见过它，不过人们对它向来称许有加。」

===Original===
They turned in at the gates of Stormwood. Though the trees and bushes were gaunt and bare the grass already showed stretches of vivid green. In the fading light one caught glimpses through the shrubbery of the lake beyond. It was a dazzling sapphire blue in the sunset. A final turn of the drive. An avenue of trees. A house, massive, pillared, porticoed. The door opened as they drew up at the entrance. A maid in cap and apron stood in the doorway. A man appeared at the side of the car, coming seemingly from nowhere, greeted Paula civilly and drove the car off. The glow of an open fire in the hall welcomed them. “He’ll bring up your bag,” said Paula. “How’re the babies, Anna? Has Mr. Storm got here?”

===Chinese===
他们在斯托姆伍德的大门口拐了进去。树木灌丛虽然还光秃嶙峋，草地却已透出一摊摊鲜绿。渐暗的天光里，透过灌木能瞥见远处的湖。湖水在落日下是那种晃眼的蓝宝石蓝。车道最后一拐。一条树木夹道的路。一幢宅子，厚重，带廊柱，带门廊。他们在门口停稳，门应声而开。一个戴帽系围裙的女仆站在门里。一个男人在车旁冒了出来，简直像凭空出现的，客客气气地向宝拉致意，把车开走了。门厅里敞炉的火光迎着他们。「他会把你的行李袋送上去，」宝拉说。「娃娃们都好吧，安娜（Anna）？斯托姆先生到了吗？」

===Original===
“He telephoned, Mrs. Storm. He says he won’t be out till late﻿—maybe ten or after. Anyway, you’re not to wait dinner.”

===Chinese===
「他来过电话，斯托姆太太。他说他要很晚才到得了——也许十点，也许十点过后。反正，您不必等他吃饭。」

===Original===
Paula, from being the limp, expert, fearless driver of the high-powered roadster was now suddenly very much the mistress of the house, quietly observant, giving an order with a lift of the eyebrow or a nod of the head. Would Dirk like to go to his room at once? Perhaps he’d like to look at the babies before they went to sleep for the night, though the nurse would probably throw him out. One of those stern British females. Dinner at seven-thirty. He needn’t dress. Just as he liked. Everything was very informal here. They roughed it. (Dirk had counted thirteen servants by noon next day and hadn’t been near the kitchen, laundry, or dairy.)

===Chinese===
宝拉方才还是那辆大马力跑车里软洋洋、熟练、无畏的驾驶者，这会儿忽然十足是个宅子的女主人，静悄悄地察看着一切，抬一抬眉毛、点一点头就把吩咐发了出去。德克想不想这就进自己房间去？也许他愿意趁娃娃们夜里睡下之前去看看他们，虽然保姆多半会把他轰出来。就是那种铁面无私的英国女人。七点半开饭。他不必换礼服。全随他的便。这儿一切都不拘客套。他们过的是苦日子。（到第二天中午，德克已经数出了十三个佣人，而且还没走近过厨房、洗衣房和奶房一步。）

===Original===
His room, when he reached it, he thought pretty awful. A great square chamber with narrow leaded windows, deep-set, on either side. From one he could get a glimpse of the lake, but only a glimpse. Evidently the family bedrooms were the lake rooms. In the DeJong code and class the guest had the best but evidently among these moneyed ones the family had the best and the guest was made comfortable, but was not pampered. It was a new angle for Dirk. He thought it startling but rather sensible. His bag had been brought up, unpacked, and stowed away in a closet before he reached his room. “Have to tell that to Selina,” he thought, grinning. He looked about the room, critically. It was done in a style that he vaguely defined as French. It gave him the feeling that he had stumbled accidentally into the chamber of a Récamier and couldn’t get out. Rose brocade with gold net and cream lace and rosebuds. “Swell place for a man,” he thought, and kicked a footstool﻿—a *fauteuil* he supposed it was called, and was secretly glad that he could pronounce it faultlessly. Long mirrors, silken hanging, cream walls. The bed was lace hung. The coverlet was rose satin, feather-light. He explored his bathroom. It actually was a room, much larger than his alcove bedroom on Deming Place﻿—as large as his own bedroom at home on the farm. The bath was done dazzlingly in blue and white. The tub was enormous and as solid as if the house had been built around it. There were towels and towels and towels in blue and white, ranging in size all the way from tiny embroidered wisps to fuzzy all-enveloping bath towels as big as a carpet.

===Chinese===
他到了自己房间一看，觉得相当吓人。一间巨大的方形卧房，两侧是窄窄的铅条格子窗，深深地凹在墙里。从其中一扇能望见一线湖景，但也只是一线。自家的卧室显然才是望湖的房间。按德容家的规矩和阶层，最好的房间该归客人；可在这些有钱人中间，分明是自家占最好的，客人呢，也让你舒舒服服，但绝不娇惯。这对德克是个新角度。他觉得骇人，倒也相当在理。还没等他走到房间，他的行李袋已经给送上来、拆了包、收进壁橱了。「这个得讲给塞莉娜听。」他想，咧嘴一笑。他挑剔地打量这房间。它的路数他含含糊糊地断定为法国风。它给他的感觉是：自己一失足闯进了哪位雷卡米耶夫人（Récamier）的闺房，而且出不去了。玫瑰色锦缎，配金色网纱、米色花边和玫瑰花蕾。「真是个爷们儿住的好地方。」他想，一脚踢在一个脚凳上——他猜这物件叫 *fauteuil*（法语：扶手椅），并且暗自得意，自己能把它念得一字不差。长穿衣镜，丝质帷幔，米色的墙。床上挂着花边。床罩是玫瑰色缎子，轻若羽毛。他去探看自己的浴室。那真是名副其实的一间屋子，比他迪明广场那间凹室卧室大多了——跟家里农场他自己那间卧室一般大。浴室是蓝白两色，亮得晃眼。浴缸大得吓人，敦实得像整座房子是绕着它盖起来的。蓝的白的毛巾一摞又一摞，尺寸从小小的绣花细丝片，一直到毛茸茸、能把人整个裹住、有地毯那么大的浴巾，无所不有。

===Original===
He was much impressed.

===Chinese===
他大为叹服。

===Original===
He decided to bathe and change into dinner clothes and was glad of this when he found Paula in black chiffon before the fire in the great beamed room she had called the library. Dirk thought she looked very beautiful in that diaphanous stuff, with the pearls. Her heart-shaped face, with its large eyes that slanted a little at the corners; her long slim throat; her dark hair piled high and away from her little ears. He decided not to mention it.

===Chinese===
他决定先洗澡，换上晚餐礼服；等他看见宝拉穿着黑雪纺、站在炉火前——在那间她称作书房的、梁木粗大的屋子里——他为这个决定暗自庆幸。德克觉得她裹在那层薄纱里、戴着那串珍珠，看上去美极了。她那张心形脸，一双大眼睛，眼角微微上挑；她那颀长纤细的脖颈；她那乌黑的头发高高堆起，露出小小的耳朵。他决定不去提这一茬。

===Original===
“You look extremely dangerous,” said Paula.

===Chinese===
「你看上去危险极了。」宝拉说。

===Original===
“I am,” replied Dirk, “but it’s hunger that brings this look of the beast to my usually mild Dutch features. Also, why do you call this the library?” Empty shelves gaped from the wall on all sides. The room was meant to hold hundreds of volumes. Perhaps fifty or sixty in all now leaned limply against each other or lay supine.

===Chinese===
「我是危险，」德克答道，「不过是我素来温和的荷兰人面孔上，饿出了一股兽相。另外，你干吗管这间屋子叫书房？」空书架从四面墙上张着大口。这屋子原是打算装几百上千册书的。如今拢共大约五六十本，软塌塌地你倚着我、我靠着你，或者干脆仰面朝天躺着。

===Original===
Paula laughed. “They do look sort of sparse, don’t they? Theodore bought this place, you know, as is. We’ve books enough in town, of course. But I don’t read much out here. And Theodore!﻿—I don’t believe he ever in his life read anything but detective stories and the newspapers.”

===Chinese===
宝拉笑了。「它们看着是有点儿稀稀落落，对吧？西奥多买这地方的时候，你知道，是连屋子带东西原样盘下来的。城里的书自然尽够了。可我在乡下不大读书。至于西奥多！——我看他这一辈子，除了侦探小说和报纸，什么都没读过。」

===Original===
Dirk told himself that Paula had known her husband would not be home until ten and had deliberately planned a tête-à-tête meal. He would not, therefore, confess himself a little nettled when Paula said, “I’ve asked the Emerys in for dinner; and we’ll have a game of bridge afterward. Phil Emery, you know, the Third. He used to have it on his visiting card, like royalty.”

===Chinese===
德克心里明白，宝拉早知道她丈夫十点前回不来，是存心安排一顿两人对坐的饭。因此，当宝拉说「我请了埃默里一家（the Emerys）来吃饭；饭后我们打一场桥牌。菲尔·埃默里，你知道的，三世。从前他把这名头印在拜客名片上，跟王室似的」的时候，他也不肯承认自己有一点着恼。

===Original===
The Emerys were drygoods; had been drygoods for sixty years; were accounted Chicago aristocracy; preferred England; rode to hounds in pink coats along Chicago’s prim and startled suburban prairies. They had a vast estate on the lake near Stormwood. They arrived a trifle late. Dirk had seen pictures of old Phillip Emery (“Phillip the First,” he thought, with an inward grin) and decided, looking at the rather anemic third edition, that the stock was running a little thin. Mrs. Emery was blonde, statuesque, and unmagnetic. In contrast Paula seemed to glow like a sombre jewel. The dinner was delicious but surprisingly simple; little more than Selina would have given him, Dirk thought, had he come home to the farm this weekend. The talk was desultory and rather dull. And this chap had millions, Dirk said to himself. Millions. No scratching in an architect’s office for this lad. Mrs. Emery was interested in the correct pronunciation of Chicago street names.

===Chinese===
埃默里家是做布匹杂货生意的；布匹杂货做了六十年；算得上芝加哥的世家望族；偏爱英国；穿着猩红猎装，在芝加哥那些一本正经、受宠若惊的郊区草原上纵马猎狐。他们在斯托姆伍德附近的湖边有一大片庄园。他们到得稍稍迟了些。德克见过老菲利普·埃默里的照片（「菲利普一世。」他心里暗笑），再看看眼前这多少有点贫血的第三代，断定这份家系的血统是越传越薄了。埃默里太太金发白肤，体态如雕像，却全无磁性。相形之下，宝拉倒像一颗幽暗的宝石，灼灼放光。饭菜可口，却简单得出奇；德克想，比他这个周末若是回农场去塞莉娜给他张罗的也多不了多少。谈话东拉西扯，相当乏味。而这小子有的是几百万，德克对自己说。几百万哪。这小子用不着在建筑师事务所里伏案苦熬。埃默里太太感兴趣的，是芝加哥街名的正确读音。

===Original===
“It’s terrible,” she said. “I think there ought to be a Movement for the proper pronunciation. The people ought to be taught; and the children in the schools. They call Goethe Street ‘Gerty’; and pronounce all the *s*’s in Des Plaines. Even Illinois they call ‘Illi*noise*.’ ” She was very much in earnest. Her breast rose and fell. She ate her salad rapidly. Dirk thought that large blondes oughn’t to get excited. It made their faces red.

===Chinese===
「太不像话了，」她说。「我看应该发起一场正音运动。应该教教老百姓，还有学校里的孩子。他们把歌德街（Goethe Street）念成『格蒂』；念德斯普兰斯街（Des Plaines）的时候，连词尾的 *s* 都一个不落全念出来。连伊利诺伊（Illinois），他们都念成『伊利*噪*伊』。」她极为恳切。胸脯一起一伏。她飞快地吃着沙拉。德克想，金发的大块头女人不该激动。激动了脸要红。

===Original===
At bridge after dinner Phillip the Third proved to be sufficiently the son of his father to win from Dirk more money than he could conveniently afford to lose. Though Mrs. Phil had much to do with this, as Dirk’s partner. Paula played with Emery, a bold shrewd game.

===Chinese===
饭后打桥牌，菲利普三世充分证明了他不愧是他父亲的儿子，从德克手里赢去的钱，超过了德克输起来不伤元气的数目。虽说菲尔太太作为德克的搭档，对此出力甚多。宝拉跟埃默里一伙，打一手大胆精明的牌。

===Original===
Theodore Storm came in at ten and stood watching them. When the guests had left the three sat before the fire. “Something to drink?” Storm asked Dirk. Dirk refused but Storm mixed a stiff highball for himself, and then another. The whiskey brought no flush to his large white impassive face. He talked almost not at all. Dirk, naturally silent, was loquacious by comparison. But while there was nothing heavy, unvital about Dirk’s silence this man’s was oppressive, irritating. His paunch, his large white hands, his great white face gave the effect of bleached bloodless bulk. “I don’t see how she stands him,” Dirk thought. Husband and wife seemed to be on terms of polite friendliness. Storm excused himself and took himself off with a word about being tired, and seeing them in the morning.

===Chinese===
西奥多·斯托姆十点钟进来，站在旁边看他们打牌。客人走后，三个人在炉火前坐下。「喝点什么？」斯托姆问德克。德克谢绝了，斯托姆却给自己兑了一大杯浓烈的威士忌海波（highball），接着又是一杯。威士忌没能让那张苍白的大脸泛起一丝血色。他几乎一言不发。德克天生寡言，比起来倒显得话多了。不过德克的沉默里没有沉重，没有死气，这个人的沉默却压得人慌，惹得人烦。他的便便大腹，他的白色大手，他的白色大脸，给人一种漂白过的、没有血色的庞大身量的感觉。「真不知道她怎么受得了他。」德克想。夫妻俩似乎维持着一种客客气气的友好。斯托姆说了声乏了、明早见，告个便，自去了。

===Original===
After he had gone: “He likes you,” said Paula.

===Chinese===
他走之后：「他喜欢你。」宝拉说。

===Original===
“Important,” said Dirk, “if true.”

===Chinese===
「要紧得很哪，」德克说，「如果属实的话。」

===Original===
“But it is important. He can help you a lot.”

===Chinese===
「可它就是属实。他能帮你大忙。」

===Original===
“Help me how? I don’t want﻿—”

===Chinese===
「帮什么忙？我可不想要——」

===Original===
“But I do. I want you to be successful. I want you to be. You can be. You’ve got it written all over you. In the way you stand, and talk, and don’t talk. In the way you look at people. In something in the way you carry yourself. It’s what they call force, I suppose. Anyway, you’ve got it.”

===Chinese===
「可我想要。我要你成功。我要你成。你能成。你通身上下都写着这一层。在你站立的姿势里，说话的姿势里，不说话的姿势里。在你看人的眼神里。在你举手投足的某种东西里。人家管这个叫魄力，大概吧。反正，你有。」

===Original===
“Has your husband got it?”

===Chinese===
「你丈夫有吗？」

===Original===
“Theodore! No! That is﻿—”

===Chinese===
「西奥多！没有！也就是说——」

===Original===
“There you are. I’ve got the force, but he’s got the money.”

===Chinese===
「这就结了。我有的是魄力，他有钱。」

===Original===
“You can have both.” She was leaning forward. Her eyes were bright, enormous. Her hands﻿—those thin dark hot hands﻿—were twisted in her lap. He looked at her quietly. Suddenly there were tears in her eyes. “Don’t look at me that way, Dirk.” She huddled back in her chair, limp. She looked a little haggard and older, somehow. “My marriage is a mess, of course. You can see that.”

===Chinese===
「你可以两样都有。」她身子前倾。眼睛亮亮的，睁得极大。她的双手——那双瘦削、黝黑、滚烫的手——在膝上绞着。他静静地望着她。忽然她眼里涌上了泪水。「别那样看着我，德克。」她软软地瘫回椅子里。不知怎么，她显得有点憔悴，也见老了。「我的婚姻是一团糟，这自然。你也看得出来。」

===Original===
“You knew it would be, didn’t you?”

===Chinese===
「你当初就知道会这样，不是吗？」

===Original===
“No. Yes. Oh, I don’t know. Anyway, what’s the difference, now? I’m not trying to be what they call an Influence in your life. I’m just fond of you﻿—you know that﻿—and I want you to be great and successful. It’s maternal, I suppose.”

===Chinese===
「不。是。唉，我不知道。反正事到如今，还有什么两样？我没打算当你人生里人家所谓的『感化力』（Influence）。我就是喜欢你——这你知道——我要你了不起，要你成功。大概是一种母性吧。」

===Original===
“I should think two babies would satisfy that urge.”

===Chinese===
「我还以为，生了两个娃娃，这股劲儿也就使够了呢。」

===Original===
“Oh, I can’t get excited about two pink healthy lumps of babies. I love them and all that, but all they need is to have a bottle stuffed into their mouths at proper intervals and to be bathed, and dressed and aired and slept. It’s a mechanical routine and about as exciting as a treadmill. I can’t go round being maternal and beating my breast over two nice firm lumps of flesh.”

===Chinese===
「唉，两团粉红结实的娃娃肉，我可激动不起来。我爱他们，这些都好说，可他们要的，不过是到时候往嘴里塞奶瓶，洗澡，穿衣，透气，睡觉。一套机械的例行公事，跟踩踏车一样没劲。我没法整天泛着母性，对着两块好好儿的、结结实实的肉团捶胸顿足。」

===Original===
“Just what do you want me to do, Paula?”

===Chinese===
「你到底要我干什么，宝拉？」

===Original===
She was eager again, vitally concerned in him. “It’s all so ridiculous. All these men whose incomes are thirty﻿—forty﻿—sixty﻿—a hundred thousand a year usually haven’t any qualities, really, that the five-thousand-a-year man hasn’t. The doctor who sent Theodore a bill for four thousand dollars when each of my babies was born didn’t do a thing that a country doctor with a Ford wouldn’t do. But he knew he could get it and he asked it. Somebody has to get the fifty-thousand-dollar salaries﻿—some advertising man, or bond salesman or﻿—why, look at Phil Emery! He probably couldn’t sell a yard of pink ribbon to a schoolgirl if he had to. Look at Theodore! He just sits and blinks and says nothing. But when the time comes he doubles up his fat white fist and mumbles, ‘Ten million,’ or ‘Fifteen million,’ and that settles it.”

===Chinese===
她又热切起来，把全副心思都放在他身上。「这一切真是可笑极了。这些年收入三万——四万——六万——十万美元的男人，说实在的，通常并没有什么能耐是年薪五千的男人所没有的。我每生下一个娃娃，那位大夫就给西奥多开一张四千美元的账单，而他做的事，没有一件是哪个开着福特车的乡下大夫做不了的。可他知道自己收得上来，他就要了。总得有人拿那五万美元的年薪——什么广告人啦，债券推销员啦，或者——嘿，瞧瞧菲尔·埃默里！真要他自己动手，他多半连一码粉红缎带都卖不给一个女学生。瞧瞧西奥多！他只会干坐着眨眼，一言不发。可到了节骨眼上，他捏起那只肥白的拳头，含混地吐出一句『一千万』，或者『一千五百万』，事情就算定了。」

===Original===
Dirk laughed to hide his own little mounting sensation of excitement. “It isn’t quite as simple as that, I imagine. There’s more to it than meets the eye.”

===Chinese===
德克笑起来，好掩盖自己心头那点越蹿越高的兴奋。「我看事情没那么简单。这里头的名堂，比眼睛看见的多。」

===Original===
“There isn’t! I tell you I know the whole crowd of them. I’ve been brought up with this moneyed pack all my life, haven’t I? Pork packers and wheat grabbers and peddlers of gas and electric light and dry goods. Grandfather’s the only one of the crowd that I respect. He has stayed the same. They can’t fool him. He knows he just happened to go into wholesale beef and pork when wholesale beef and pork was a new game in Chicago. Now look at him!”

===Chinese===
「就是那么简单！我告诉你，这一整帮人我全认得。我从小就是跟这帮有钱的货色一块儿长大的，不是吗？猪肉加工商，抢麦子的，贩煤气、贩电灯、贩布匹杂货的。这一伙人里，我敬重的只有外公。他一直是老样子。他们糊弄不了他。他自己心里有数：他不过是赶巧在批发牛肉猪肉还算芝加哥一门新行当的时候，进了批发牛肉猪肉这一行。如今瞧瞧他！」

===Original===
“Still, you will admit there’s something in knowing when,” he argued.

===Chinese===
「不过你总得承认，懂得什么时候下手，这里头有讲究。」他争道。

===Original===
Paula stood up. “If you don’t know I’ll tell you. Now is when. I’ve got Grandfather and Dad and Theodore to work with. You can go on being an architect if you want to. It’s a fine enough profession. But unless you’re a genius where’ll it get you! Go in with them, and Dirk, in five years﻿—”

===Chinese===
宝拉站起身。「你不知道，我告诉你。就是现在。我背后有外公，有爸爸，有西奥多三个人可搭手。你要愿意，尽管去当你的建筑师。这也是个够体面的行当。可你要不是天才，它能把你带到哪儿去！跟他们一块儿干，还有，德克，五年之内——」

===Original===
“What!” They were both standing, facing each other, she tense, eager; he relaxed but stimulated.

===Chinese===
「什么！」两人都站着，面对面，她紧张，热切；他松弛着，却受了撩拨。

===Original===
“Try it and see what, will you? Will you, Dirk?”

===Chinese===
「试一试，看看能怎样，好不好？你肯不肯，德克？」

===Original===
“I don’t know, Paula. I should say my mother wouldn’t think much of it.”

===Chinese===
「我不知道，宝拉。要我说，我母亲对这种事不会怎么瞧得上。」

===Original===
“What does she know! Oh, I don’t mean that she isn’t a fine, wonderful person. She is. I love her. But success! She thinks success is another acre of asparagus or cabbage; or a new stove in the kitchen now that they’ve brought gas out as far as High Prairie.”

===Chinese===
「她懂什么！哦，我不是说她不是个好人、不是个了不起的人。她是。我爱她。可是成功！她以为成功就是多一英亩芦笋或者卷心菜；如今煤气都通到高草原了，就是厨房里添个新炉子。」

===Original===
He had a feeling that she possessed him; that her hot eager hands held him though they stood apart and eyed each other almost hostilely.

===Chinese===
他有一种感觉：她占有了他；她那双滚烫急切的手攥住了他——虽然两人分开站着，几乎带着敌意你瞪着我、我瞪着你。

===Original===
As he undressed that night in his rose and satin room he thought, “Now what’s her game? What’s she up to? Be careful, Dirk, old boy.” On coming into the room he had gone immediately to the long mirror and had looked at himself carefully, searchingly, not knowing that Paula, in her room, had done the same. He ran a hand over his close-shaved chin, looked at the fit of his dinner coat. He wished he had had it made at Peter Peel’s, the English tailor on Michigan Boulevard. But Peel was so damned expensive. Perhaps next time﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
当夜他在那间玫瑰缎子的房间里脱衣裳时想：「她打的是什么主意？她想干什么？当心，德克老弟。」进屋的时候，他头一件事就是走到长穿衣镜前，仔仔细细、搜搜寻寻地端详自己，并不知道宝拉在她自己房里也照做了。他一只手摸摸刮得干干净净的下巴，看看晚礼服上身合不合体。他真希望这套衣服当初是在彼得·皮尔那儿做的——密歇根林荫大道上的那位英国裁缝。可皮尔贵得要死。也许下回……

===Original===
As he lay in the soft bed with the satin coverlet over him he thought, “Now what’s her lit‑tle game!”

===Chinese===
他躺在软床上，身上盖着玫瑰缎床罩，心里想：「这会儿她又打的是什么小‑算盘！」

===Original===
He awoke at eight, enormously hungry. He wondered, uneasily, just how he was going to get his breakfast. She had said his breakfast would be brought him in his room. He stretched luxuriously, sprang up, turned on his bath water, bathed. When he emerged in dressing gown and slippers his breakfast tray had been brought him mysteriously and its contents lay appetizingly on a little portable table. There were flocks of small covered dishes and a charming individual coffee service. The morning papers, folded and virgin, lay next this. A little note from Paula: “Would you like to take a walk at about half-past nine? Stroll down to the stables. I want to show you my new horse.”

===Chinese===
他八点醒来，饿得发慌。他心里不无忐忑地琢磨，这早饭究竟怎么个吃法。她说过，早饭会送到他房间里来。他美滋滋地伸了个大懒腰，跳起来，放好洗澡水，洗了澡。等他穿着晨衣、趿着拖鞋出来，早餐托盘已经神不知鬼不觉地送来了，吃食摆在一架小折桌上，样样开胃。一大队带盖小碟，一套讨喜的单人咖啡用具。旁边搁着晨报，折得方方正正，还没拆封。宝拉的一张小便条：「九点半左右愿意出来散步吗？溜达到马厩来。我要给你看看我的新马。」

===Original===
The distance from the house to the stables was actually quite a brisk little walk in itself. Paula, in riding clothes, was waiting for him. She looked boyish and young standing beside the sturdy bulk of Pat, the head stableman. She wore tan whipcord breeches, a coat of darker stuff, a little round felt hat whose brim curved away from her face.

===Chinese===
从宅子到马厩这段路，本身就够快步走上好一阵的。宝拉穿着骑装等他。她站在马厩总管帕特（Pat）那敦实的身量旁边，显得男孩子气，年轻得很。她穿一条黄褐色斜纹马裤，一件深色料子上衣，一顶小小的圆毡帽，帽檐从她脸旁往外弯开。

===Original===
She greeted him. “I’ve been out two hours. Had my ride.”

===Chinese===
她招呼了他。「我已经出去两个钟头了。骑过了。」

===Original===
“I hate people who tell you, first thing in the morning, that they’ve been out two hours.”

===Chinese===
「我最恨一大早头一句话就告诉你他已出去两个钟头的人。」

===Original===
“If that’s the kind of mood you’re in we won’t show him the horse, will we, Pat?”

===Chinese===
「你要是这股脾气，咱们就不给他看马了，对不对，帕特？」

===Original===
Pat thought they would. Pat showed him the new saddle mare as a mother exhibits her latest offspring, tenderly, proudly. “Look at her back,” said Pat. “That’s the way you tell a horse, sir. By the length of this here line. Lookut it! There’s a picture for you, now!”

===Chinese===
帕特觉得可以看。帕特领他看那匹新来的鞍骑母马，像母亲展示自己最新生的孩子，又温柔，又得意。「瞧她这脊背，」帕特说。「相马就靠这个，先生。就瞧这条线的长短。你瞧瞧！这才叫一个漂亮，喏！」

===Original===
Paula looked up at Dirk. “You ride, don’t you?”

===Chinese===
宝拉抬头望德克。「你会骑马，是不是？」

===Original===
“I used to ride the old nags, bareback, on the farm.”

===Chinese===
「从前在农场，我常骑那些老驽马，光着马背骑。」

===Original===
“You’ll have to learn. We’ll teach him, won’t we, Pat?”

===Chinese===
「那你也得学。咱们来教他，好不好，帕特？」

===Original===
Pat surveyed Dirk’s lean, flexible figure. “Easy.”

===Chinese===
帕特把德克那精瘦灵巧的身条打量了一番。「容易。」

===Original===
“Oh, say!” protested Dirk.

===Chinese===
「嗐，瞧你说的！」德克抗声道。

===Original===
“Then I’ll have someone to ride with me. Theodore never rides. He never takes any sort of exercise. Sits in that great fat car of his.”

===Chinese===
「那我就有人陪我骑马了。西奥多从来不骑马。他什么运动都不来。就窝在他那辆又大又肥的汽车里。」

===Original===
They went into the coach house, a great airy whitewashed place with glittering harness and spurs and bridles like jewels in glass cases. There were ribbons, too, red and yellow and blue in a rack on the wall; and trophy cups. The coach house gave Dirk a little hopeless feeling. He had never before seen anything like it. In the first place, there were no motors in it. He had forgotten that people rode in anything but motors. A horse on Chicago’s boulevards raised a laugh. The sight of a shining brougham with two sleek chestnuts driving down Michigan Avenue would have set that street to staring and sniggering as a Roman chariot drawn by zebras might have done. Yet here was such a brougham, glittering, spotless. Here was a smart cream surrey with a cream-coloured top hung with fringe. There were two-wheeled carts high and slim and chic. A victoria. Two pony carts. One would have thought, seeing this room, that the motor vehicle had never been invented. And towering over all, dwarfing the rest, out-glittering them, stood a tally-ho, a sheer piece of wanton insolence. It was in perfect order. Its cushions were immaculate. Its sides shone. Its steps glistened. Dirk, looking up at it, laughed outright. It seemed too splendid, too absurd. With a sudden boyish impulse he swung himself up the three steps that led to the box and perched himself on the fawn cushioned seat. He looked very handsome there. “A coach and four﻿—isn’t that what they call it? Got any Roman juggernauts?”

===Chinese===
他们走进马车房——一间高大轩敞、刷得雪白的屋子，亮闪闪的马具、马刺和辔头像珠宝似的陈在玻璃柜里。还有绶带，红的黄的蓝的，插在墙上的架子里；还有奖杯。这马车房叫德克生出一点绝望的感觉。他从没见过这样的阵仗。头一件，里头一辆汽车也没有。他都忘了世上还有人坐汽车以外的东西出门。在芝加哥的林荫大道上出现一匹马，会招人哄笑。一辆锃亮的布鲁厄姆式马车（brougham）由两匹毛色油光的栗色马拉着驶下密歇根大道，那条街准会看得目瞪口呆、嗤嗤窃笑，跟斑马拉的罗马战车驶过去一个样。可这儿就停着一辆这样的布鲁厄姆，亮闪闪，一尘不染。这儿是一辆时髦的奶油色四轮敞篷马车（surrey），奶油色的车顶垂着流苏。有几辆高挑瘦长、别致入时的两轮轻马车。一辆维多利亚式敞篷马车。两辆小矮马马车。看了这间屋子，你会以为汽车压根儿没被发明过。而高踞一切之上、把其余的都比得矮小、比得黯然失色的，是一辆塔利霍四马大马车（tally-ho）——十足一件肆无忌惮的炫富摆谱。它收拾得完美无瑕。坐垫干干净净。车身锃亮。踏级闪光。德克仰头望着它，放声大笑。它太华奢，也太荒唐了。他忽然起了一阵孩子气的冲动，三步蹿上通向车夫座的踏级，一屁股坐在浅黄褐色的坐垫上。他坐在那儿，非常英俊。「四驾大马车——人家是这么叫的吧？你们还有罗马的贾格纳特神车没有？」

===Original===
“Do you want to drive it?” asked Paula. “This afternoon? Do you think you can? Four horses, you know.” She laughed up at him, her dark face upturned to his.

===Chinese===
「你想赶赶它吗？」宝拉问。「今天下午？你觉得你行吗？你可得知道，是四匹马哟。」她仰起黝黑的脸冲他笑，脸朝上对着他的脸。

===Original===
Dirk looked down at her. “No.” He climbed down. “I suppose that at about the time they drove this hereabouts my father was taking the farm plugs into the Haymarket.”

===Chinese===
德克低头看她。「不。」他爬下来。「我估摸着，当年人家在这一带赶这车兜风的年月，我父亲正赶着农场的驽马上海马克特去呢。」

===Original===
Something had annoyed him, she saw. Would he wait while she changed to walking things? Or perhaps he’d rather drive in the roadster. They walked up to the house together. He wished that she would not consult his wishes so anxiously. It made him sulky, impatient.

===Chinese===
有什么东西惹恼他了，她看出来了。她换步行装，他愿意等吗？或者他宁可坐跑车去。他们一起往宅子走。他但愿她别这么急煎煎地事事问他。这叫他憋闷，叫他不耐烦。

===Original===
She put a hand on his arm. “Dirk, are you annoyed at me for what I said last night?”

===Chinese===
她把一只手搭上他的胳膊。「德克，你是为昨晚我说的那些话在恼我吗？」

===Original===
“No.”

===Chinese===
「不是。」

===Original===
“What did you think when you went to your room last night? Tell me. What did you think?”

===Chinese===
「昨晚你回房之后都想什么了？告诉我。你想了什么？」

===Original===
“I thought: ‘She’s bored with her husband and she’s trying to vamp me. I’ll have to be careful.’ ”

===Chinese===
「我想：『她跟丈夫过腻了，想勾住我。我得留神。』」

===Original===
Paula laughed delightedly. “That’s nice and frank﻿ ﻿… What else?”

===Chinese===
宝拉开怀大笑。「坦白得可爱……还有呢？」

===Original===
“I thought my coat didn’t fit very well and I wished I could afford to have Peel make my next one.”

===Chinese===
「我还想，我这件上衣不大合身，恨不得攒得起钱，叫皮尔给我做下一件。」

===Original===
“You can,” said Paula.

===Chinese===
「你可以的。」宝拉说。

## XVI / 第十六章

===Original===
As it turned out, Dirk was spared the necessity of worrying about the fit of his next dinner coat for the following year and a half. His coat, during that period, was a neat olive drab as was that of some millions of young men of his age, or thereabouts. He wore it very well, and with the calm assurance of one who knows that his shoulders are broad, his waist slim, his stomach flat, his flanks lean, and his legs straight. Most of that time he spent at Fort Sheridan, first as an officer in training, then as an officer training others to be officers. He was excellent at this job. Influence put him there and kept him there even after he began to chafe at the restraint. Fort Sheridan is a few miles outside Chicago, north. No smart North Shore dinner was considered complete without at least a major, a colonel, two captains, and a sprinkling of first lieutenants. Their boots shone so delightfully while dancing.

===Chinese===
后来事情是这样：此后一年半，德克倒用不着操心下一件晚礼服合不合身了。那段时间里，他的外衣是一身挺括的橄榄绿军呢，同几百万与他年纪相仿或不相上下的年轻人穿的一样。这身衣裳他穿得十分好看，带着一种从容的自信，仿佛深知自己肩宽、腰细、腹平、胁瘦、腿直。那段日子的大半，他在谢里登堡（Fort Sheridan）度过，先是受训的军官，后来是训练别人当军官的军官。这差事他干得漂亮极了。是门路把他送到那儿，又把他留在那儿，哪怕后来他已开始对那份约束感到烦躁。谢里登堡在芝加哥城外以北几英里。北岸体面的晚宴，席间若没有至少一位少校、一位上校、两位上尉，再零零星星缀上几名少尉，便算不得圆满。跳舞的时候，他们的军靴闪起光来，多喜人。

===Original===
In the last six months of it (though he did not, of course, know that it was to be the last six months) Dirk tried desperately to get to France. He was suddenly sick of the neat job at home; of the dinners; of the smug routine; of the olive-drab motor car that whisked him wherever he wanted to go (he had a captaincy); of making them “snap into it”; of Paula; of his mother, even. Two months before the war’s close he succeeded in getting over; but Paris was his headquarters.

===Chinese===
在最后六个月里（当然，他并不知道那将是最后六个月），德克拼命想法子去法国。他忽然腻透了家里这份安稳差事；腻透了晚宴；腻透了那套自满的例行公事；腻透了那辆载着他呼啸来去、想去哪儿就去哪儿的橄榄绿汽车（他已是上尉）；腻透了冲着人喝一声「快着点」；腻透了宝拉；甚至连他的母亲也腻透了。战争结束前两个月，他总算过去了；只是他的大本营在巴黎。

===Original===
Between Dirk and his mother the first rift had appeared.

===Chinese===
德克和母亲之间，出现了第一道裂痕。

===Original===
“If I were a man,” Selina said, “I’d make up my mind straight about this war and then I’d do one of two things. I’d go into it the way Jan Snip goes at forking the manure pile﻿—a dirty job that’s got to be cleaned up; or I’d refuse to do it altogether if I didn’t believe in it as a job for me. I’d fight, or I’d be a conscientious objector. There’s nothing in between for anyone who isn’t old or crippled, or sick.”

===Chinese===
「我要是个男人，」塞莉娜说，「这场战争我立刻拿定主意，然后二者必居其一。要么就像扬·斯尼普叉粪堆那样一头扎进去——那是件脏活，可总得有人收拾干净；要么，我若不信这是该我干的活儿，就干脆一概拒绝。我要么去打仗，要么做个良心拒服兵役者（conscientious objector）。凡是不老、不残、不病的人，中间的道路是没有的。」

===Original===
