Paula was aghast when she heard this. So was Julie whose wailings had been loud when Eugene had gone into the air service. He was in France now, thoroughly happy. “Do you mean,” demanded Paula, “that you actually want Dirk to go over there and be wounded or killed!”

===Chinese===
宝拉听了这话大惊失色。朱莉也一样——当初尤金进航空队，她哭喊得惊天动地。他如今在法国，快活得很。「你的意思是，」宝拉质问，「你当真想让德克到那边去，去挂彩、去送命！」

===Original===
“No. If Dirk were killed my life would stop. I’d go on living, I suppose, but my life would have stopped.”

===Chinese===
「不。德克若是死了，我的生命就停了。人我想还会活下去，可我的生命已经停了。」

===Original===
They all were doing some share in the work to be done.

===Chinese===
该做的事，人人都尽了一份力。

===Original===
Selina had thought about her own place in this war welter. She had wanted to do canteen work in France but had decided against this as being selfish. “The thing for me to do,” she said, “is to go on raising vegetables and hogs as fast as I can.” She supplied countless households with free food while their men were gone. She herself worked like a man, taking the place of the able-bodied helper who had been employed on her farm.

===Chinese===
塞莉娜也想好了自己在这场战乱滚滚中的位置。她本想去法国做战地食堂的工作，又觉得那未免自私，便作罢了。「我该做的，」她说，「就是接着种我的菜、喂我的猪，能多快就多快。」男人们不在家的日子，她白白供应了不知多少户人家吃食。她自己像个男人一样干活，顶上了原先农场雇的那名壮劳力。

===Original===
Paula was lovely in her Red Cross uniform. She persuaded Dirk to go into the Liberty Bond selling drive and he was unexpectedly effective in his quiet, serious way; most convincing and undeniably thrilling to look at in uniform. Paula’s little air of possession had grown until now it enveloped him. She wasn’t playing now; was deeply and terribly in love with him.

===Chinese===
宝拉穿红十字会的制服很美。她劝德克参加自由公债（Liberty Bond）推销运动，他以他那安静严肃的路数干得出人意料地见效；穿上军服，看上去极有说服力，也确实动人心魄。宝拉那点占有姿态如今滋长成这样：把他整个人罩了进去。她这回不是玩玩而已；她深深爱上了他，爱得厉害。

===Original===
When, in 1918, Dirk took off his uniform he went into the bond department of the Great Lakes Trust Company in which Theodore Storm had a large interest. He said that the war had disillusioned him. It was a word you often heard uttered as a reason or an excuse for abandoning the normal. “Disillusioned.”

===Chinese===
到了 1918 年德克脱下军装，进了五大湖信托公司（Great Lakes Trust Company）的债券部，西奥多·斯托姆在这家公司里有大宗股份。他说这场战争使他幻想破灭了。这个词你常常听人说，当作抛开正业的理由或者借口。「幻灭了。」

===Original===
“What did you think war was going to do?” said Selina. “Purify! It never has yet.”

===Chinese===
「你以为战争能干什么呢？」塞莉娜说。「净化！它从来没净化过。」

===Original===
It was understood, by Selina at least, that Dirk’s abandoning of his profession was a temporary thing. Quick as she usually was to arrive at conclusions, she did not realize until too late that this son of hers had definitely deserted building for bonds; that the only structures he would rear were her own castles in Spain. His first two months as a bond salesman netted him more than a year’s salary at his old post at Hollis & Sprague’s. When he told this to Selina, in triumph, she said, “Yes, but there isn’t much fun in it, is there? This selling things on paper? Now architecture, that must be thrilling. Next to writing a play and seeing it acted by real people﻿—seeing it actually come alive before your eyes﻿—architecture must be the next most fun. Putting a building down on paper﻿—little marks here, straight lines there, figures, calculations, blueprints, measurements﻿—and then, suddenly one day, the actual building itself. Steel and stone and brick, with engines throbbing inside it like a heart, and people flowing in and out. Part of a city. A piece of actual beauty conceived by you! Oh, Dirk!” To see her face then must have given him a pang, it was so alive, so eager.

===Chinese===
至少在塞莉娜看来，德克抛开本行不过是暂时的。她素来敏于下结论，可直到为时已晚才明白：她这个儿子已经斩钉截铁地弃了建筑、就了债券；他往后唯一还会去营造的构筑物，是她自己的西班牙空中楼阁。他做债券推销员的头两个月，进项就超过了在霍利斯与斯普拉格建筑师事务所旧职一年的薪水。他把这个当成大捷讲给塞莉娜听，她却说：「是啊，可这事儿没什么乐趣，对不对？就这么在纸上卖卖东西？建筑可就不一样了，那才叫人心头发热。除了写一出戏、看着它由活人演出来——看着它当真在你眼前活起来——就该数建筑最有意思了。把一座楼安放在纸上——这儿几个小记号，那儿几条直线，数字、计算、蓝图、尺寸——然后忽然有一天，楼本身立起来了。钢铁、石头、砖，里面机器像心脏一样突突跳动，人流进进出出。一座城的一部分。一件由你孕育出来的实实在在的美！啊，德克！」那时若看到她的脸，必定叫他心头一痛：那张脸那么鲜活，那么热切。

===Original===
He found excuses for himself. “Selling bonds that make that building possible isn’t so dull, either.”

===Chinese===
他给自己找辙。「卖出的债券能把那样一座楼盖起来，也不算乏味。」

===Original===
But she waved that aside almost contemptuously. “What nonsense, Dirk. It’s like selling seats at the box office of a theatre for the play inside.”

===Chinese===
可她几乎不屑地把这话挥开了。「胡说，德克。这就好比在戏院售票处卖座儿，卖的是里头那出戏。」

===Original===
Dirk had made many new friends in the last year and a half. More than that, he had acquired a new manner; an air of quiet authority, of assurance. The profession of architecture was put definitely behind him. There had been no building in all the months of the war; probably would be none in years. Materials were prohibitive, labour exorbitant. He did not say to Selina that he had put the other work from him. But after six months in his new position he knew that he would never go back.

===Chinese===
这一年半里德克交了许多新朋友。不止如此，他还添了一副新仪态；一种安静的威严、笃定的气派。建筑这一行，算是断然撂在身后了。打仗那些月份里根本没盖过房子；恐怕几年之内也不会有。材料贵得吓人，工钱高得离谱。他没有对塞莉娜说自己已把那门手艺丢开了。可是在新位子上待满六个月之后，他知道自己是再也不回去了。

===Original===
From the start he was a success. Within one year he was so successful that you could hardly distinguish him from a hundred other successful young Chicago business and professional men whose clothes were made at Peel’s; who kept their collars miraculously clean in the soot-laden atmosphere of the Loop; whose shoes were bench-made; who lunched at the Noon Club on the roof of the First National Bank where Chicago’s millionaires ate corned-beef hash whenever that plebeian dish appeared on the bill of fare. He had had a little thrill out of his first meal at this club whose membership was made up of the “big men” of the city’s financial circle. Now he could even feel a little flicker of contempt for them. He had known old Aug Hempel, of course, for years, as well as Michael Arnold, and, later, Phillip Emery, Theodore Storm, and others. But he had expected these men to be different.

===Chinese===
他一起步就成功。不出一年，他成功得叫你几乎分不清他和芝加哥城里另外一百个成功的年轻商界与专业界人士有什么两样——衣裳在皮尔那儿定做；在卢普区煤烟弥漫的空气里领子始终奇迹般干净；鞋子出自工作台手工精制；午饭到第一国民银行（First National Bank）楼顶的午间俱乐部（Noon Club）去吃——每逢菜单上出现咸牛肉杂烩这道平民菜，芝加哥的百万富翁们必要点它。头一回在这家俱乐部吃饭，他心里还有几分小小的激动，这里的会员全是本市金融界的「大人物」。如今他甚至能觉出一星对他们的轻蔑。老奥格·亨佩尔他当然认识多年了，还有迈克尔·阿诺德，后来是菲利普·埃默里、西奥多·斯托姆，还有别几位。可他原以为这些人该与众不同才是。

===Original===
Paula had said, “Theodore, why don’t you take Dirk up to the Noon Club some day? There are a lot of big men he ought to meet.”

===Chinese===
宝拉说过：「西奥多，哪天你把德克带上午间俱乐部去不好吗？有一大批大人物他该见见。」

===Original===
Dirk went in some trepidation. The great grilled elevator, as large as a room, whisked them up to the roof of the fortress of gold. The club lounge furnished his first disappointment. It looked like a Pullman smoker. The chairs were upholstered in black leather or red plush. The woodwork was shiny red imitation mahogany. The carpet was green. There were bright shining brass cuspidors in the hall near the cigar counter. The food was well cooked. Man’s food. Nine out of every ten of these men possessed millions. Whenever corned beef and cabbage appeared on the luncheon menu nine out of ten took it. These were not at all the American Big Business Man of the comic papers and of fiction﻿—that yellow, nervous, dyspeptic creature who lunches off milk and pie. They were divided into two definite types. The older men of between fifty and sixty were great high-coloured fellows of full habit. Many of them had had a physician’s warning of high blood pressure, hardening arteries, overworked heart, rebellious kidneys. So now they waxed cautious, taking time over their substantial lunches, smoking and talking. Their faces were impassive, their eyes shrewd, hard. Their talk was colloquial and frequently illiterate. They often said “was” for “were.” “Was you going to see Baldwin about that South American stuff or is he going to ship it through without?” Most of them had known little of play in their youth and now they played ponderously and a little sadly and yet eagerly as does one to whom the gift of leisure had come too late. On Saturday afternoon you saw them in imported heather green golf stockings and Scotch tweed suits making for the links or the lake. They ruined their palates and livers with strong cigars, thinking cigarette smoking undignified and pipes common. “Have a cigar!” was their greeting, their password, their open sesame. “Have a cigar.” Only a few were so rich, so assured as to smoke cheap light panatellas. Old Aug Hempel was one of these. Dirk noticed that when he made one of his rare visits to the Noon Club his entrance was met with a little stir, a deference. He was nearing seventy-five now; was still straight, strong, zestful of life; a magnificent old buccaneer among the pettier crew. His had been the direct and brutal method﻿—swish! swash! and his enemies walked the plank. The younger men eyed him with a certain amusement and respect.

===Chinese===
德克去时心里颇有点打鼓。那部带格栅的大电梯，大得像间屋子，把他们径直送上黄金堡垒的顶楼。俱乐部的休息厅给了他头一个失望。它活像普尔曼火车的吸烟车厢（Pullman smoker）。椅子蒙的是黑皮或红长毛绒。木装是亮闪闪的红漆假红木。地毯是绿的。雪茄柜旁的过厅里摆着黄铜痰盂，锃光瓦亮。菜烧得好。男人的饭食。这些人十个有九个身家百万。午餐菜单上一出现咸牛肉煮卷心菜，十个有九个必点。他们同滑稽画报和小说里那个美国大企业家毫不相干——那种面黄、神经质、消化不良的家伙，午饭只吃牛奶和馅饼。他们分明是两类人。五六十岁上下的年长者，个个红光满面、体态丰硕。其中不少人得过医生的警告：血压高了，血管硬了，心脏过劳了，肾脏造反了。于是如今他们学得谨慎了，对着丰盛的午饭细嚼慢咽，抽烟，谈话。他们的脸不动声色，眼睛精明、冷酷。他们谈吐俚俗，还常常不合文法。他们爱把 were 说成 was。「Was you going to see Baldwin about that South American stuff or is he going to ship it through without?」（你到底打算去见鲍德温谈那批南美货，还是他不经接头就直接发运？）他们大多数人年轻时几乎没怎么玩过，如今玩起来沉甸甸的、有点伤怀，却又急切，就像闲暇这份礼物来得太迟的人那样。星期六下午，你看得见他们穿着进口的石南绿高尔夫长袜、苏格兰粗花呢衣裤，奔高尔夫球场或者湖边去。烈性雪茄毁了他们的味蕾和肝脏，他们却嫌抽纸烟有失身份、抽烟斗失之俗气。「来支雪茄！」是他们的见面礼，他们的口令，他们的芝麻开门。「来支雪茄。」只有寥寥几位，才阔气到、笃定到抽那种便宜清淡的小雪茄（panatellas）。老奥格·亨佩尔便是其一。德克注意到，他难得来一回午间俱乐部，一进门便激起一阵小小的骚动、一片敬意。他年近七十五了；腰板仍直，筋骨仍壮，对生活仍兴味盎然；在一群小喽罗中间，是位威风凛凛的老海盗。他的路数素来是直截了当、血淋淋的那一套——唰！嚓！他的对头便一个个走了跳板。年轻一辈看他，目光里带着几分好笑，也有几分敬重。

===Original===
These younger men whose ages ranged from twenty-eight to forty-five were disciples of the new system in business. They were graduates of universities. They had known luxury all their lives. They were the second or third generation. They used the word “psychology.” They practised restraint. They knew the power of suggestion. Where old Aug Hempel had flown the black flag they resorted to the periscope. Dirk learned that these men did not talk business during meal time except when they had met definitely for that purpose. They wasted a good deal of time, Dirk thought, and often, when they were supposed to be “in conference” or when their secretaries said primly that they were very busy and not to be disturbed until three, they were dozing off for a comfortable half hour in their private offices. They were the sons or grandsons of those bearded, rugged, and rather terrible old boys who, in 1835 or 1840, had come out of County Limerick or County Kilkenny or out of Scotland or the Rhineland to mold this new country in their strong hairy hands; those hands whose work had made possible the symphony orchestras, the yacht clubs, the golf clubs through which their descendants now found amusement and relaxation.

===Chinese===
这些更年轻的人，年纪从二十八到四十五不等，是生意场上新体系的信徒。他们都是大学毕业。他们生来就享惯了奢华。他们是第二代、第三代。他们嘴里用「心理学」这个词。他们讲克制。他们懂得暗示的力量。老奥格·亨佩尔扯起黑旗硬抢的地方，他们改用潜望镜。德克这才知道，这些人吃饭时不谈生意，除非特意为此聚在一起。德克心想，他们浪费的光阴可着实不少：明明该在「开会」，或者秘书一板一眼地说他非常忙、三点以前请勿打扰，他却正在私人办公室里舒舒服服打上半个钟头的盹。他们是那帮蓄胡子、粗砺、相当可怕的半老头子的儿子或孙子——那些人在 1835 或 1840 年间，从利默里克郡（County Limerick）或基尔肯尼郡（County Kilkenny），从苏格兰或莱茵兰（the Rhineland）出来，用一双多毛的有力大手塑造这个新国家；正是那双手的劳作，才使交响乐团、游艇俱乐部、高尔夫俱乐部成为可能——他们的子孙如今便在这些地方寻欢作乐、松弛身心。

===Original===
Dirk listened to the talk of the Noon Club.

===Chinese===
德克听午间俱乐部里的谈话。

===Original===
“I made it in eighty-six. That isn’t so bad for the Tippecanoe course.”

===Chinese===
「我这轮打了八十六。在蒂珀卡努（Tippecanoe）球场，这不算坏。」

===Original===
“… boxes are going pretty well but the Metropolitan grabs up all the big ones and the house wants names. Garden doesn’t draw the way she used to, even in Chicago. It’s the popular subscription that counts.”

===Chinese===
「……包厢卖得挺好，可大包厢全叫大都会歌剧院（Metropolitan）抢了去，剧院方面要的是名角。加登（Garden）已不复当年叫座，在芝加哥也一样。要紧的是大众订票。」

===Original===
“… grabbed the Century out of New York at two-forty-five and got back here in time to try out my new horse in the park. She’s a little nervous for city riding but we’re opening the house at Lake Forest next week﻿—”

===Chinese===
「……赶两点四十五分的『世纪号』（the Century）从纽约过来，回到这儿正好来得及上公园试试我的新马。跑城里的路她有点儿毛躁，不过咱们下礼拜就上莱克福里斯特开宅子了——」

===Original===
“… pretty good show but they don’t send the original companies here, that’s the trouble﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「……戏倒是不错，可人家不把原班人马送到咱们这儿来，毛病就出在这儿……」

===Original===
“… in London. It’s a neat shade of green, isn’t it? You can’t get ties like this over here, I don’t know why. Got a dozen last time I was over. Yeh, Plumbridge in Bond Street.”

===Chinese===
「……在伦敦买的。这绿色多雅致，是不是？这样的领带这边买不着，我不知道为什么。上回过去买了一打。可不是嘛，邦德街（Bond Street）普拉姆布里奇（Plumbridge）家的。」

===Original===
Well, Dirk could talk like that easily enough. He listened quietly, nodded, smiled, agreed or disagreed. He looked about him carefully, appraisingly. Waist lines well kept in; carefully tailored clothes; shrewd wrinkles of experience radiating in fine sprays in the skin around the corners of their eyes. The president of an advertising firm lunching with a banker; a bond salesman talking to a rare book collector; a packer seated at a small table with Horatio Craft, the sculptor.

===Chinese===
哼，这套话德克说来也毫不费力。他安静地听着，点头，微笑，或同意或不同意。他细细地、掂量地打量四周。腰身收得很好；衣裳裁剪考究；眼角四周的皮肤上，精明的阅历皱纹像细密的水花般放射开来。一位广告公司总经理在陪银行家吃饭；一位债券推销员在同珍本书收藏家谈话；一位肉品加工商坐在小桌旁，对面是雕塑家霍雷肖·克拉夫特（Horatio Craft）。

===Original===
Two years and Dirk, too, had learned to “grab the Century” in order to save an hour or so of time between Chicago and New York. Peel said it was a pleasure to fit a coat to his broad, flat tapering back, and trousers to his strong sturdy legs. His colour, inherited from his red-cheeked Dutch ancestors brought up in the fresh sea-laden air of the Holland flats, was fine and clear. Sometimes Selina, in pure sensuous delight, passed her gnarled, work-worn hand over his shoulders and down his fine, strong, straight back. He had been abroad twice. He learned to call it “running over to Europe for a few days.” It had all come about in a scant two years, as is the theatrical way in which life speeds in America.

===Chinese===
两年过去，德克也学会了「赶世纪号」，好在芝加哥与纽约之间省下一两个钟头。皮尔说，给他那宽阔平直、往下收窄的背配上衣，给他那两条结结实实的腿配裤子，是件乐事。他那副气色，得自红脸颊的荷兰祖先——他们在荷兰低地饱含海气的新鲜空气里长大——细腻而明净。有时塞莉娜纯粹出于官能的愉悦，把她那双疙疙瘩瘩、劳损的手抚上他的肩头，顺着他那美好、结实、笔直的脊背滑下去。他出过两趟国。他学会了管这叫「溜到欧洲去待几天」。这一切拢共不到两年工夫就成了——美国的生活就是这样，踩着戏剧的锣鼓点往前赶。

===Original===
Selina was a little bewildered now at this new Dirk whose life was so full without her. Sometimes she did not see him for two weeks, or three. He sent her gifts which she smoothed and touched delightedly and put away; fine soft silken things, handmade﻿—which she could not wear. The habit of years was too strong upon her. Though she had always been a woman of dainty habits and fastidious tastes the grind of her early married life had left its indelible mark. Now, as she dressed, you might have seen that her petticoat was likely to be black sateen and her plain, durable corset cover neatly patched where it had worn under the arms. She employed none of the artifices of a youth-mad day. Sun and wind and rain and the cold and heat of the open prairie had wreaked their vengeance on her flouting of them. Her skin was tanned, weather-beaten; her hair rough and dry. Her eyes, in that frame, startled you by their unexpectedness, they were so calm, so serene, yet so alive. They were the beautiful eyes of a wise young girl in the face of a middle-aged woman. Life was still so fresh to her.

===Chinese===
如今这个新的德克，生活里没有她也排得满满的，塞莉娜不由得有点发怔。有时她两三个星期见不着他一面。他送她礼物，她欢欢喜喜地摩挲抚弄一番，再收起来；细软的丝绸物件，手工做的——她穿不出去。多年积下的习惯在她身上太深了。她虽然从来是个举止洁净、口味挑剔的女人，早年婚后那些年的苦熬却留下了抹不掉的印记。如今她穿衣裳时你会看见：她的衬裙多半是黑棉缎的，那件朴素结实的紧身胸衣外罩，腋下磨破的地方补得整整齐齐。这个为青春发狂的时代的一切巧计，她一样不用。日头、风、雨和空旷草原上的严寒酷暑，曾为她的小看而向她报了仇。她的皮肤晒得黢黑，风霜满布；头发又粗又干。可是在那样的框子里，她的眼睛偏偏叫你吃一惊——那么沉静，那么安详，却又那么鲜活。那是一双聪明少女的美丽眼睛，长在一个中年妇人的脸上。生活于她还是这么新鲜。

===Original===
She had almost poignantly few personal belongings. Her bureau drawers were like a nun’s; her brush and comb, a scant stock of plain white underwear. On the bathroom shelf her toothbrush, some vaseline, a box of talcum powder. None of those aids to artifice with which the elderly woman deludes herself into thinking that she is hoodwinking the world. She wore well-made walking oxfords now, with sensible heels﻿—the kind known as Field’s special; plain shirtwaists and neat dark suits, or a blue cloth dress. A middle-aged woman approaching elderliness; a woman who walked and carried herself well; who looked at you with a glance that was direct but never hard. That was all. Yet there was about her something arresting, something compelling. You felt it.

===Chinese===
她的私人物件少得近乎令人心酸。五斗柜的抽屉像修女的：一把梳子一把头刷，一小叠素白内衣。浴室搁板上一支牙刷，一点凡士林，一盒爽身粉。上了年纪的女人用来自欺、以为哄过了全世界的那些道具，她一样也没有。如今她穿做工扎实的牛津式走路鞋，鞋跟平实——就是人称菲尔德特制款（Field's special）的那种；素色女衬衫，整洁的深色套裙，或者一身蓝布衣裙。一个临近老年的中年妇人；一个走路、坐立都有仪态的妇人；她看你的那一眼直截了当，却从不严苛。如此而已。可她身上自有一种叫人注目、迫人的东西。你感觉得到。

===Original===
“I don’t see how you do it!” Julie Arnold complained one day as Selina was paying her one of her rare visits in town. “Your eyes are as bright as a baby’s and mine look like dead oysters.” They were up in Julie’s dressing room in the new house on the north side﻿—the new house that was now the old house. Julie’s dressing table was a bewildering thing. Selina DeJong, in her neat black suit and her plain black hat, sat regarding it and Julie seated before it, with a grim and lively interest.

===Chinese===
「我真不明白你是怎么弄的！」一天，塞莉娜难得进城来看她，朱莉·阿诺德抱怨道。「你的眼睛亮得像婴孩，我的眼睛活像两只死牡蛎。」她们在城北那座新宅——如今已成旧宅的新宅——楼上朱莉的梳妆间里。朱莉的梳妆台是件叫人眼花缭乱的东西。塞莉娜·德容一身整洁的黑套裙，一顶素净的黑帽，坐在那儿端详它，端详坐在台前的朱莉，神情又冷峻又起劲。

===Original===
“It looks,” Selina said, “like Mandel’s toilette section, or a hospital operating room just before a major operation.” There were great glass jars that contained meal, white and gold. There were rows and rows of cream pots holding massage cream, vanishing cream, cleansing cream. There were little china bowls of scarlet and white and yellowish pastes. A perforated container spouted a wisp of cotton. You saw toilet waters, perfumes, atomizers, French soaps, unguents, tubes. It wasn’t a dressing table merely, but a laboratory.

===Chinese===
「这架势，」塞莉娜说，「像曼德尔百货（Mandel's）的化妆部，又像大手术前一刻的手术室。」白瓷描金的大玻璃罐里盛着香粉。一排排一排排的膏罐：按摩霜、无痕霜、洁面霜。小瓷碗里盛着猩红的、雪白的、淡黄的膏糊。一个带孔的罐子里探出一绺药棉。你看见化妆水、香水、喷雾器、法国香皂、香膏、软管。这已不止是一张梳妆台，是一间实验室。

===Original===
“This!” exclaimed Julie. “You ought to see Paula’s. Compared to her toilette ceremony mine is just a splash at the kitchen sink.” She rubbed cold cream now around her eyes with her two forefingers, using a practised upward stroke.

===Chinese===
「这算什么！」朱莉叫道。「你该看看宝拉的。比起她那套梳妆大典，我这点儿不过是厨房水池里撩把水。」说着她用两根食指往眼圈上抹冷霜，手劲娴熟地朝上推。

===Original===
“It looks fascinating,” Selina exclaimed. “Some day I’m going to try it. There are so many things I’m going to try some day. So many things I’ve never done that I’m going to do for the fun of it. Think of it, Julie! I’ve never had a manicure! Some day I’m going to have one. I’ll tell the girl to paint my nails a beautiful bright vermilion. And I’ll tip her twenty-five cents. They’re so pretty with their bobbed hair and their queer bright eyes. I s’pose you’ll think I’m crazy if I tell you they make me feel young.”

===Chinese===
「瞧着真叫人心痒，」塞莉娜说。「哪天我也要来试一试。有多少事我都想着哪天试一试。多少我从来没做过的事，我要做来图个乐子。你想想，朱莉！我从来没修过指甲！哪天我要去修一回。我要叫那姑娘给我指甲涂上漂漂亮亮的鲜朱红。我还要赏她两毛五的小费。她们剪着短发，亮晶晶的眼睛怪有趣的，多俊呀。我要是跟你说，她们让我觉得自己年轻了，你准得想我是疯了。」

===Original===
Julie was massaging. Her eyes had an absent look. Suddenly: “Listen, Selina. Dirk and Paula are together too much. People are talking.”

===Chinese===
朱莉按摩着。眼神有点恍惚。忽然她说：「听着，塞莉娜。德克和宝拉在一起太勤了。人家在讲闲话呢。」

===Original===
“Talking?” The smile faded from Selina’s face.

===Chinese===
「讲闲话？」塞莉娜脸上的笑意褪了下去。

===Original===
“Goodness knows I’m not straitlaced. You can’t be in this day and age. If I had ever thought I’d live to see the time when﻿—Well, since the war of course anything’s all right, seems. But Paula has no sense. Everybody knows she’s insane about Dirk. That’s all right for Dirk, but how about Paula! She won’t go anywhere unless he’s invited. Of course Dirk is awfully popular. Goodness knows there are few enough young men like him in Chicago﻿—handsome and successful and polished and all. Most of them dash off East just as soon as they can get their fathers to establish an Eastern branch or something.﻿ ﻿… They’re together all the time, everywhere. I asked her if she was going to divorce Storm and she said no, she hadn’t enough money of her own and Dirk wasn’t earning enough. His salary’s thousands, but she’s used to millions. Well!”

===Chinese===
「天知道我可不是个古板人。如今这个年头谁也古板不成。我要是想过自己能活着见到这种世道——唉，自从打了仗，自然，怎么着似乎都不碍事了。可宝拉没有分寸。谁都知道她为德克疯了。德克倒不碍事，可宝拉怎么办！他不受请的地方她一概不去。当然，德克红得不得了。天知道，芝加哥像他这样的年轻男人本就没几个——又俊、又得意、又有教养，样样齐全。他们大都是一撺掇老爹在东部开个分号什么的，就一溜烟奔东边去了。……他们俩时时刻刻黏在一起，到处都是。我问她是不是打算跟斯托姆离婚，她说不，她自己手里的钱不够，德克挣的也不够。他的薪水论千，她过惯的却是论百万。唉！」

===Original===
“They were boy and girl together,” Selina interrupted, feebly.

===Chinese===
「他们到底是小时候一起玩大的男孩和女孩。」塞莉娜插了一句，有气无力。

===Original===
“They’re not any more. Don’t be silly, Selina. You’re not as young as that.”

===Chinese===
「早不是了。别犯傻了，塞莉娜。你没那么年轻了。」

===Original===
No, she was not as young as that. When Dirk next paid one of his rare visits to the farm she called him into her bedroom﻿—the cool, dim shabby bedroom with the old black walnut bed in which she had lain as Pervus DeJong’s bride more than thirty years ago. She had on a little knitted jacket over her severe white nightgown. Her abundant hair was neatly braided in two long plaits. She looked somehow girlish there in the dim light, her great soft eyes gazing up at him.

===Chinese===
是的，她没那么年轻了。等德克下一次难得回农场来，她把他叫进自己卧房——那间凉丝丝、暗沉沉、寒碜的卧室，摆着那张老黑胡桃木床；三十多年前，她作为珀维斯·德容的新娘，就在这张床上躺过。她那件素严的白睡衣外头罩了件小编织衫。一头浓发齐齐整整编作两条长辫。暗光里她不知怎么竟有点像个姑娘，一双温柔的大眼睛仰望着他。

===Original===
“Dirk, sit down here at the side of my bed the way you used to.”

===Chinese===
「德克，过来坐在我床边，像你从前行的那样。」

===Original===
“I’m dead tired, Mother. Twenty-seven holes of golf before I came out.”

===Chinese===
「我累死了，妈。来之前打了二十七洞高尔夫。」

===Original===
“I know. You ache all over﻿—a nice kind of ache. I used to feel like that when I’d worked in the fields all day, pulling vegetables, or planting.” He was silent. She caught his hand. “You didn’t like that. My saying that. I’m sorry. I didn’t say it to make you feel bad, dear.”

===Chinese===
「我知道。你浑身都酸——酸得受用。我从前在地里干上一整天活——拔菜呀，点种呀——就是那种感觉。」他不做声。她抓住他的手。「你不喜欢这话。我这么说。对不起。我不是存心叫你难受的，亲爱的。」

===Original===
“I know you didn’t, Mother.”

===Chinese===
「我知道您不是，妈。」

===Original===
“Dirk, do you know what that woman who writes the society news in the Sunday *Tribune* called you today?”

===Chinese===
「德克，你知道星期天的《论坛报》（the Sunday *Tribune*）上写社交新闻的那个女人，今天管你叫什么吗？」

===Original===
“No. What? I never read it.”

===Chinese===
「不知道。叫什么？我从来不看那版。」

===Original===
“She said you were one of the *jeunesse dorée*.”

===Chinese===
「她说你是 *jeunesse dorée*（法语：镀金青年）之流。」

===Original===
Dirk grinned. “Gosh!”

===Chinese===
德克咧嘴一笑。「嚯！」

===Original===
“I remember enough of my French at Miss Fister’s school to know that that means gilded youth.”

===Chinese===
「我在菲斯特学校学的那点法语还够使，知道这话的意思就是镀金青年。」

===Original===
“Me! That’s good! I’m not even spangled.”

===Chinese===
「我！真逗！我连亮片都没贴一片。」

===Original===
“Dirk!” her voice was low, vibrant. “Dirk, I don’t want you to be a gilded youth, I don’t care how thick the gilding. Dirk, that isn’t what I worked in the sun and cold for. I’m not reproaching you; I didn’t mind the work. Forgive me for even mentioning it. But, Dirk, I don’t want my son to be known as one of the *jeunesse dorée*. No! Not my son!”

===Chinese===
「德克！」她的嗓音低沉、发颤。「德克，我不要你做什么镀金青年，金箔贴得多厚都不要。德克，我在日头底下、在严寒里做活，不是为的这个。我不是责备你；那活儿我不在乎干。原谅我，连提都不该提的。可是德克，我不愿人家把我儿子当成 *jeunesse dorée*（镀金青年）里的一个。不！我的儿子不能！」

===Original===
“Now, listen, Mother. That’s foolish. If you’re going to talk like that. Like a mother in a melodrama whose son’s gone wrong.﻿ ﻿… I work like a dog. You know that. You get the wrong angle on things, stuck out here on this little farm. Why don’t you come into town and take a little place and sell the farm?”

===Chinese===
「行了，您听着，妈。这是犯傻。您要这么说的话。说得跟闹剧里的母亲似的，儿子学坏了的那种。……我干活干得像条狗。这您知道。您守在这小农场上，看事情的角度都看偏了。您干吗不进城去，找个小住处，把这农场卖了？」

===Original===
“Live with you, you mean?” Pure mischievousness.

===Chinese===
「您的意思是，跟您一起住？」纯属调皮。

===Original===
“Oh, no. You wouldn’t like that,” hastily. “Besides, I’d never be there. At the office all day, and out somewhere in the evening.”

===Chinese===
「哦，不。您不会喜欢的，」他赶忙说。「再说，我也压根儿不在家。白天一整天在办公室，晚上又在外头什么地方。」

===Original===
“When do you do your reading, Dirk?”

===Chinese===
「您如今什么时候读书呢，德克？」

===Original===
“Why﻿—uh﻿—”

===Chinese===
「呃——嗯——」

===Original===
She sat up in bed, looking down at the thin end of her braid as she twined it round and round her finger. “Dirk, what is this you sell in that mahogany office of yours? I never did get the hang of it.”

===Chinese===
她在床上坐起来，低头看着辫子梢，把它在手指上一圈一圈绕。「德克，你那间红木办公室里卖的到底是什么？我始终没弄懂那门道。」

===Original===
“Bonds, Mother. You know that perfectly well.”

===Chinese===
「债券，妈。这您清楚得很。」

===Original===
“Bonds.” She considered this a moment. “Are they hard to sell? Who buys them?”

===Chinese===
「债券。」她琢磨了一会儿。「难卖吗？都谁买呢？」

===Original===
“That depends. Everybody buys them﻿—that is﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「看情况。人人都买——就是说……」

===Original===
“I don’t. I suppose because whenever I had any money it went back into the farm for implements, or repairs, or seed, or stock, or improvements. That’s always the way with a farmer﻿—even on a little truck farm like this.” She pondered again a moment. He fidgeted, yawned. “Dirk DeJong﻿—Bond Salesman.”

===Chinese===
「我就不买。我猜是因为，我手里一有钱，就又投回农场里去了——置农具呀，修缮呀，买种子呀，添牲口呀，搞改良呀。庄稼人从来是这样——哪怕像我这样小小的蔬菜农场。」她又寻思了一会儿。他坐不住，打了个哈欠。「德克·德容——债券推销员。」

===Original===
“The way you say it, Mother, it sounds like a low criminal pursuit.”

===Chinese===
「照您这个说法，妈，听着倒像门下三滥的犯罪营生了。」

===Original===
“Dirk, do you know sometimes I actually think that if you had stayed here on the farm﻿—”

===Chinese===
「德克，你知道吗，有时候我当真会想，要是你当年留在这农场上——」

===Original===
“Good God, Mother! What for!”

===Chinese===
「老天爷，妈！图什么呀！」

===Original===
“Oh, I don’t know. Time to dream. Time to﻿—no, I suppose that isn’t true any more. I suppose the day is past when the genius came from the farm. Machinery has cut into his dreams. He used to sit for hours on the wagon seat, the reins slack in his hands, while the horses plodded into town. Now he whizzes by in a jitney. Patent binders, ploughs, reapers﻿—he’s a mechanic. He hasn’t time to dream. I guess if Lincoln had lived today he’d have split his rails to the tune of a humming, snarling patent wood cutter, and in the evening he’d have whirled into town to get his books at the public library, and he’d have read them under the glare of the electric light bulb instead of lying flat in front of the flickering wood fire.﻿ ﻿… Well.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「哦，我说不上。有工夫做梦。有工夫——不，我想这已经不实在了。天才出自农场的日子，我想是过去了。机器把他的梦剪短了。他从前能在货车座上坐上几个钟头，缰绳松松垮垮攥在手里，由着马一步一步磨进城里去。如今他坐着吉特尼（jitney，早期简易公共汽车）嗖地飞过去。专利打捆机、犁、收割机——他成了个机修工。他没有工夫做梦了。我想林肯若是活在今天，他劈栅栏木也得伴着一台嗡嗡咆哮的专利劈木机，到了晚上，他得旋风似的赶进城，上公共图书馆去取书，在电灯泡白晃晃的强光底下读，而不是趴在闪动的柴火跟前。……唉。……」

===Original===
She lay back, looked up at him. “Dirk, why don’t you marry?”

===Chinese===
她躺回去，仰脸望着他。「德克，你怎么还不结婚？」

===Original===
“Why﻿—there’s no one I want to marry.”

===Chinese===
「这个——没有我想娶的人。」

===Original===
“No one who’s free, you mean?”

===Chinese===
「你是说，没有能娶的人？」

===Original===
He stood up. “I mean no one.” He stooped and kissed her lightly. Her arms went round him close. Her hand with the thick gold wedding band on it pressed his head to her hard. “Sobig!” He was a baby again.

===Chinese===
他站起身。「我是说没有。」他俯身轻轻吻了她。她的双臂紧紧搂住他。她那只戴着厚厚金婚戒的手，把他的头紧紧按在自己身上。「这么大！」他又成了个小娃娃。

===Original===
“You haven’t called me that in years.” He was laughing.

===Chinese===
「您都有多少年没这么叫过我了。」他笑着。

===Original===
She reverted to the old game they had played when he was a child. “How big is my son! How big?” She was smiling, but her eyes were sombre.

===Chinese===
她又拾起他小时候两人玩熟的那个老游戏。「我的儿子有多大！有多大？」她微笑着，眼睛却是黯淡的。

===Original===
“So big!” answered Dirk, and measured a very tiny space between thumb and forefinger. “So big.”

===Chinese===
「这—么大！」德克答道，拇指和食指间比出极小极小的一段。「这—么大。」

===Original===
She faced him, sitting up very straight in bed, the little wool shawl hunched about her shoulders. “Dirk, are you ever going back to architecture? The war is history. It’s now or never with you. Pretty soon it will be too late. Are you ever going back to architecture? To your profession?”

===Chinese===
她面对着他，在床上坐得笔直，小小的羊毛披巾拱在肩头。「德克，你还回得去搞建筑吗？战争已经是历史了。你要么现在回去，要么永远回不去。不久就太迟了。你还回得去搞建筑吗？回到你的本行去？」

===Original===
A clean amputation. “No, Mother.”

===Chinese===
干净利落的一刀。「不回了，妈。」

===Original===
She gave an actual gasp, as though icy water had been thrown full in her face. She looked suddenly old, tired. Her shoulders sagged. He stood in the doorway, braced for her reproaches. But when she spoke it was to reproach herself. “Then I’m a failure.”

===Chinese===
她真真切切倒抽了一口气，仿佛一盆冰水兜头泼在脸上。她一下子显老、显倦了。肩膀塌了下来。他站在门口，准备着挨她的责备。可她开口时，责备的却是自己。「那我就是个失败的人了。」

===Original===
“Oh, what nonsense, Mother. I’m happy. You can’t live somebody else’s life. You used to tell me, when I was a kid I remember, that life wasn’t just an adventure, to be taken as it came, with the hope that something glorious was always hidden just around the corner. You said you had lived that way and it hadn’t worked. You said﻿—”

===Chinese===
「哦，多没道理，妈。我快活。人不能替别人过一辈子。我记得还是小孩子的时候，您常跟我说，生活不光是一场冒险，不能走一步看一步，总指望着什么辉煌的东西就藏在下一个拐角上。您说您那样活过来过，结果行不通。您还说——」

===Original===
She interrupted him with a little cry. “I know I did. I know I did.” Suddenly she raised a warning finger. Her eyes were luminous, prophetic. “Dirk, you can’t desert her like that!”

===Chinese===
她轻轻叫了一声，打断他。「我知道我说过。我知道我说过。」忽然她竖起一根警示的手指。她的眼睛发亮，像先知的眼睛。「德克，你不能就这样抛弃她！」

===Original===
“Desert who?” He was startled.

===Chinese===
「抛弃谁？」他吓了一跳。

===Original===
“Beauty! Self-expression. Whatever you want to call it. You wait! She’ll turn on you some day. Some day you’ll want her, and she won’t be there.”

===Chinese===
「美！自我表现。随你怎么称呼她。你等着！她总有一天会跟你翻脸。有一天你会要她，而她已不在那儿了。」

===Original===
Inwardly he had been resentful of this bedside conversation with his mother. She made little of him, he thought, while outsiders appreciated his success. He had said, “So big,” measuring a tiny space between thumb and forefinger in answer to her half-playful question, but he had not honestly meant it. He thought her ridiculously old-fashioned now in her viewpoint, and certainly unreasonable. But he would not quarrel with her.

===Chinese===
对母亲这番床边的谈话，他心里其实憋着怨气。她把他看轻了，他想，外人倒还赏识他的成功。他确实说了「这—么大」，拇指和食指间比出极小的一段，来回答她半带玩笑的一问，可那不是他的真心。他觉得她如今的观点迂腐可笑，而且实在不讲道理。但他不肯同她争吵。

===Original===
“You wait, too, Mother,” he said now, smiling. “Some day your wayward son will be a real success. Wait till the millions roll in. Then we’ll see.”

===Chinese===
「您也等着瞧，妈，」此刻他微笑着说。「早晚您这个任性的儿子会成就一番真正的事业。等着几百万滚进来。到时候瞧吧。」

===Original===
She lay down, turned her back deliberately upon him, pulled the covers up about her.

===Chinese===
她躺下了，存心把背转向他，把被子拉上来裹住自己。

===Original===
“Shall I turn out your light, Mother, and open the windows?”

===Chinese===
「我给您关灯、开窗好吗，妈？」

===Original===
“Meena’ll do it. She always does. Just call her.﻿ ﻿… Good night.”

===Chinese===
「米娜会来弄。一向都是她。叫她一声就行。……晚安。」

===Original===
He knew that he had come to be a rather big man in his world. Influence had helped. He knew that, too. But he shut his mind to much of Paula’s manœuvring and wire pulling﻿—refused to acknowledge that her lean, dark, eager fingers had manipulated the mechanism that ordered his career. Paula herself was wise enough to know that to hold him she must not let him feel indebted to her. She knew that the debtor hates his creditor. She lay awake at night planning for him, scheming for his advancement, then suggested these schemes to him so deftly as to make him think he himself had devised them. She had even realized of late that their growing intimacy might handicap him if openly commented on. But now she must see him daily, or speak to him. In the huge house on Lake Shore Drive her own rooms﻿—sitting room, bedroom, dressing room, bath﻿—were as detached as though she occupied a separate apartment. Her telephone was a private wire leading only to her own bedroom. She called him the first thing in the morning; the last thing at night. Her voice, when she spoke to him, was an organ transformed; low, vibrant, with a timbre in its tone that would have made it unrecognizable to an outsider. Her words were commonplace enough, but pregnant and meaningful for her.

===Chinese===
他知道，在自己那个世界里，他已算得上一个有分量的人物。门路帮了忙。这他也知道。可宝拉的种种运筹、种种暗地牵线，他多半闭眼不看——不肯承认正是她那瘦削、黝黑、急切的手指，拨弄着支配他前程的那套机关。宝拉自己也够聪明，知道要留住他，就不能让他觉得欠她的情。她知道，欠债的人恨他的债主。她夜里睁着眼躺着，为他盘算，为他的升迁谋划，再把这些主意巧妙地说给他听，巧到让他以为是他自己想出来的。近来她甚至明白过来：他们俩日渐亲密的关系，若是被人公开议论，会拖累他。可如今她非得天天见到他，或跟他说上话不可。在湖滨大道那座大宅里，她自己那几间屋——客厅、卧室、梳妆间、浴室——自成一统，仿佛她单住着一套公寓。她的电话是一条专线，只通她自己的卧室。早晨头一件事是打电话给他；夜里最后一件事也是。她对他说起话来，嗓音变了，像一架风琴；低沉、震颤，音色叫外人听了绝认不出是她。她的话再平常不过，对她却怀满了意思，意味深长。

===Original===
“What did you do today? Did you have a good day?﻿ ﻿… Why didn’t you call me?﻿ ﻿… Did you follow up that suggestion you made about Kennedy? I think it’s a wonderful idea, don’t you? You’re a wonderful man, Dirk; did you know that?﻿ ﻿… I miss you.﻿ ﻿… Do you?﻿ ﻿… When?﻿ ﻿… Why not lunch?﻿ ﻿… Oh, not if you have a business appointment﻿ ﻿… How about five o’clock?﻿ ﻿… No, not there﻿ ﻿… Oh, I don’t know. It’s so public﻿ ﻿… Yes﻿ ﻿… Goodbye.﻿ ﻿… Good night.﻿ ﻿… Good night.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「你今天做了什么？过得好吗？……为什么不来找我？……你提的关于肯尼迪的那个建议，你跟进了吗？我看那主意妙极了，你说呢？你是个了不起的男人，德克；你知道吗？……我想你。……你想我吗？……什么时候？……一起吃午饭好不好？……哦，你要是有公务应酬就罢了……五点钟怎么样？……不，别在那儿……哦，我说不好。那儿人多眼杂……好。……再见。……晚安。……晚安。……」

===Original===
They began to meet rather furtively, in out-of-the-way places. They would lunch in department store restaurants where none of their friends ever came. They spent off afternoon hours in the dim, close atmosphere of the motion picture palaces, sitting in the back row, seeing nothing of the film, talking in eager whispers that failed to annoy the scattered devotees in the middle of the house. When they drove it was on obscure streets of the south side, as secure there from observation as though they had been in Africa, for to the north sider the south side of Chicago is the hinterland of civilization.

===Chinese===
他们开始偷偷摸摸地会面，专拣偏僻地方。午餐到百货商店的餐厅去吃，他们的朋友谁也不上那儿去。下午偷出的时辰，就消磨在电影宫昏暗闭塞的空气里，坐在最后一排，银幕上什么也没看进去，急切切地咬耳朵，也不曾搅扰场子中间稀稀落落几个影迷。开车出去，就往城南那些不起眼的街上开，在那儿躲人耳目，安稳得仿佛身在非洲——因为在城北人眼里，芝加哥的城南乃是文明的穷乡僻壤。

===Original===
Paula had grown very beautiful, her world thought. There was about her the aura, the glow, the roseate exhalation that surrounds the woman in love.

===Chinese===
宝拉出落得越发美了，她那个天地里的人都这么说。她周身有恋爱中的妇人才有的那种光晕、那种辉彩、那种玫瑰色的氤氲。

===Original===
Frequently she irritated Dirk. At such times he grew quieter than ever; more reserved. As he involuntarily withdrew she advanced. Sometimes he thought he hated her﻿—her hot eager hands, her glowing asking eyes, her thin red mouth, her sallow heart-shaped exquisite face, her perfumed clothing, her air of ownership. That was it! Her possessiveness. She clutched him so with her every look and gesture, even when she did not touch him. There was about her something avid, sultry. It was like the hot wind that sometimes blew over the prairie﻿—blowing, blowing, but never refreshing. It made you feel dry, arid, irritated, parched. Sometimes Dirk wondered what Theodore Storm thought and knew behind that impassive flabby white mask of his.

===Chinese===
她时常惹得德克恼火。这种时候他就愈发安静、愈发矜持。他不自觉地退，她便进。有时他觉得自己恨她——恨她那滚烫急切的手，那灼灼发亮、索求的眼睛，那薄薄的红嘴，那张蜡黄的、心形的、精致的脸，恨她一身的香气，她那副把人当己物的神气。就是这个！她那占有欲。她的每一个眼神、每一个举动都把他攥得死死的，哪怕碰都不碰他一下。她周身有种贪炽的、燥热的东西。像草原上有时刮起的热风——刮呀，刮呀，却从来不带来清凉。它叫你发干、焦渴、烦躁、唇焦口燥。有时德克不禁想，那张不动声色、白皙松弛的面具后头，西奥多·斯托姆在想些什么、知道些什么。

===Original===
Dirk met plenty of other girls. Paula was clever enough to see to that. She asked them to share her box at the opera. She had them at her dinners. She affected great indifference to their effect on him. She suffered when he talked to one of them.

===Chinese===
德克别的姑娘也见得多了。宝拉精明，特意安排。她请她们来分坐她歌剧院的包厢，请她们上她的晚宴。她们对他起不起作用，她装出满不在乎。他同哪一位多谈了几句，她心里便受煎熬。

===Original===
“Dirk, why don’t you take out that nice Farnham girl?”

===Chinese===
「德克，你怎么不带那位怪好的法纳姆家姑娘（Farnham）出去走走？」

===Original===
“Is she nice?”

===Chinese===
「她怪好吗？」

===Original===
“Well, isn’t she! You were talking to her long enough at the Kirks’ dance. What were you talking about?”

===Chinese===
「怎么不好！在柯克家（the Kirks）的舞会上，你跟她聊得可够久的。聊什么来着？」

===Original===
“Books.”

===Chinese===
「书。」

===Original===
“Oh. Books. She’s awfully nice and intelligent, isn’t she? A lovely girl.” She was suddenly happy. Books.

===Chinese===
「哦。书。她特别好，又有头脑，是不是？可爱的姑娘。」她一下子快活起来。是书。

===Original===
The Farnham girl was a nice girl. She was the kind of girl one should fall in love with and doesn’t. The Farnham girl was one of many well-bred Chicago girls of her day and class. Fine, honest, clearheaded, frank, capable, good-looking in an indefinite and unarresting sort of way. Hair-coloured hair, good teeth, good enough eyes, clear skin, sensible medium hands and feet; skated well, danced well, talked well. Read the books you had read. A companionable girl. Loads of money but never spoke of it. Travelled. Her hand met yours firmly﻿—and it was just a hand. At the contact no current darted through you, sending its shaft with a little zing to your heart.

===Chinese===
法纳姆家的姑娘是个好姑娘。是那种人人都该爱上而偏偏没人爱上的姑娘。她是她那一代、她那个阶层许多有教养的芝加哥姑娘中的一个。优秀、诚实、头脑清楚、坦率、能干，相貌是那种说不上来、也不惹眼的好看。头发是头发颜色的头发，牙齿好，眼睛够好，皮肤干净，手脚不大不小、恰到好处；滑冰滑得好，舞跳得好，话说得好。读的书都是你读过的。一个宜人的姑娘。有的是钱，却从不提钱。去过很多地方。她的手握住你的手，稳稳当当——那也只是一只手。指尖相触，没有电流窜过你全身，嗡的一声射进你心里。

===Original===
But when Paula showed you a book her arm, as she stood next you, would somehow fit into the curve of yours and you were conscious of the feel of her soft slim side against you.

===Chinese===
可宝拉给你看一本书的时候，她挨着你站着，那条胳膊会不知不觉嵌进你臂弯里，你会觉出她那柔软纤细的半边身子贴着你。

===Original===
He knew many girls. There was a distinct type known as the North Shore Girl. Slim, tall, exquisite; a little fine nose, a high, sweet, slightly nasal voice, earrings, a cigarette, luncheon at Huyler’s. All these girls looked amazingly alike, Dirk thought; talked very much alike. They all spoke French with a pretty good accent; danced intricate symbolic dances; read the new books; had the same patter. They prefaced, interlarded, concluded their remarks to each other with, “My deah!” It expressed, for them, surprise, sympathy, amusement, ridicule, horror, resignation. “My *deah*! You should have seen her! My *dee-ah*!”﻿—horror. Their slang was almost identical with that used by the girls working in his office. “She’s a good kid,” they said, speaking in admiration of another girl. They made a fetish of frankness. In a day when everyone talked in screaming headlines they knew it was necessary to red-ink their remarks in order to get them noticed at all. The word rot was replaced by garbage and garbage gave way to the ultimate swill. One no longer said “How shocking!” but, “How perfectly obscene!” The words, spoken in their sweet clear voices, fell nonchalantly from their pretty lips. All very fearless and uninhibited and free. That, they told you, was the main thing. Sometimes Dirk wished they wouldn’t work so hard at their play. They were forever getting up pageants and plays and large festivals for charity; Venetian fêtes, Oriental bazaars, charity balls. In the programme performance of these many of them sang better, acted better, danced better than most professional performers, but the whole thing always lacked the flavour, somehow, of professional performance. On these affairs they lavished thousands in costumes and decorations, receiving in return other thousands which they soberly turned over to the Cause. They found nothing ludicrous in this. Spasmodically they went into business or semiprofessional ventures, defying the conventions. Paula did this, too. She or one of her friends were forever opening blouse shops; starting Gifte Shoppes; burgeoning into tea rooms decorated in crude green and vermilion and orange and black; announcing their affiliation with an advertising agency. These adventures blossomed, withered, died. They were the result of postwar restlessness. Many of these girls had worked indefatigably during the 1917﻿–﻿1918 period; had driven service cars, managed ambulances, nursed, scrubbed, conducted canteens. They missed the excitement, the satisfaction of achievement.

===Chinese===
他认识的姑娘多的是。其中有一种轮廓分明的类型，人称北岸姑娘（North Shore Girl）。苗条，高挑，精致；一个小巧的鼻子，一副高而甜、微带鼻音的嗓音，耳环，一支烟，午饭在休勒氏（Huyler's）吃。这些姑娘模样像得出奇，德克想；谈吐也如出一辙。她们法语都说得腔调颇佳；跳繁复的象征舞蹈；读新出的书；一套口条个个相同。她们彼此搭话，开头、中间、结尾总缀着一句「亲耐的！」（My deah）。这三个字在她们，能表示惊讶、同情、好笑、讥诮、惊恐、听天由命。「我*亲耐*的！你真该看看她那样儿！我的*亲—耐—的*！」——这是惊恐。她们说的俚语，同他办公室里那些做事的姑娘用的几乎一模一样。「她是个好孩子，」她们带着敬佩这样夸另一个姑娘。她们把坦率奉若神明。在一个人人扯着尖叫的大字标题说话的年代，她们明白话非得蘸着红墨水说，才有人理会。「糟透了」（rot）这个词让位给「垃圾」（garbage），「垃圾」又让位于登峰造极的「泔水」（swill）。人们不再说「真是骇人！」而是说，「简直下流到家！」那些字眼由她们甜美清亮的嗓子里出来，漫不经心地从漂亮的嘴唇上滚落。全都无畏得很，百无禁忌，自由自在。这，她们告诉你，才是顶要紧的。有时德克倒希望她们玩乐的时候别那么下死力气。她们永远在张罗慈善的化装游行、演剧和盛大节庆；威尼斯游园夜会、东方义卖场、慈善舞会。在这些节目的演出里，她们中许多人唱得比多数职业演员还好，演得比职业演员还好，跳得也比职业演员还好，可整个儿不知怎么总缺一点职业演出的味道。为这些活动，她们在行头和装点上一掷千金，收回来的又是几千几千，再郑重其事地转交给「事业」。她们不觉得这里头有什么可笑之处。她们还会一阵一阵地下海经商，或干半职业的营生，公然无视成规。宝拉也干过。她或者她的哪个朋友，永远在开女衬衫铺；创办「礼品小店」（Gifte Shoppes）；忽然开出一家茶室，装潢用生猛的绿、朱红、橙和黑；或者宣布自己加盟了一家广告公司。这些营生开了花，蔫了，死了。它们是战后躁动的产物。这些姑娘中有许多在 1917—1918 年间不知疲倦地出过力；开过运输车，管过救护车，当过看护，擦洗过地板，办过战地食堂。如今她们惦念那份兴奋，惦念有所成就的满足。

===Original===
They found Dirk fair game, resented Paula’s proprietorship. Susans and Janes and Kates and Bettys and Sallys﻿—plain old-fashioned names for modern, erotic misses﻿—they talked to Dirk, danced with him, rode with him, flirted with him. His very unattainableness gave him piquancy. That Paula Storm had him fast. He didn’t care a hoot about girls.

===Chinese===
她们把德克看成正当的猎物，怨宝拉的独家专营。苏珊们、简们、凯特们、贝蒂们、萨莉们——给摩登而多情的少女们安的土里土气的老名字——跟德克说话，同他跳舞，陪他骑马，同他调情。正因为他高不可攀，才更有滋味。反正宝拉·斯托姆已把他攥得牢牢的。姑娘们他才不在乎呢。

===Original===
“Oh, Mr. DeJong,” they said, “your name’s Dirk, isn’t it? What a slick name! What does it mean?”

===Chinese===
「哦，德容先生，」她们说，「您的名字叫德克（Dirk），对不对？多俏的名字！是什么意思呀？」

===Original===
“Nothing, I suppose. It’s a Dutch name. My people﻿—my father’s people﻿—were Dutch, you know.”

===Chinese===
「我想没什么意思。是个荷兰名字。我家的人——我父亲那边的人——是荷兰人，您知道的。」

===Original===
“A dirk’s a sort of sword, isn’t it, or poniard? Anyway, it sounds very keen and cruel and fatal﻿—Dirk.”

===Chinese===
「dirk 不就是一柄短剑吗，还是匕首？反正，听着又锋利、又狠辣、又致命——德克。」

===Original===
He would flush a little (one of his assets) and smile, and look at them, and say nothing. He found that to be all that was necessary.

===Chinese===
他就微微红一红脸（这是他的一样本钱），微笑着，望着她们，一语不发。他发现，这样尽够了。

===Original===
He got on enormously.

===Chinese===
他混得风生水起。

## XVII / 第十七章

===Original===
Between these girls and the girls that worked in his office there existed a similarity that struck and amused Dirk. He said, “Take a letter, Miss Roach,” to a slim young creature as exquisite as the girl with whom he had danced the day before; or ridden or played tennis or bridge. Their very clothes were faultless imitations. They even used the same perfume. He wondered, idly, how they did it. They were eighteen, nineteen, twenty, and their faces and bodies and desires and natural equipment made their presence in a business office a paradox, an absurdity. Yet they were capable, too, in a mechanical sort of way. Theirs were mechanical jobs. They answered telephones, pressed levers, clicked buttons, tapped typewriters, jotted down names. They were lovely creatures with the minds of fourteen-year-old children. Their hair was shining, perfectly undulated, as fine and glossy and tenderly curling as a young child’s. Their breasts were flat, their figures singularly sexless like that of a very young boy. They were wise with the wisdom of the serpent. They wore wonderful little sweaters and flat babyish collars and ridiculously sensible stockings and oxfords. Their legs were slim and sturdy. Their mouths were pouting, soft, pink, the lower lip a little curled back, petal-wise, like the moist mouth of a baby that has just finished nursing. Their eyes were wide apart, empty, knowledgeous. They managed their private affairs like generals. They were cool, remote, disdainful. They reduced their boys to desperation. They were brigands, desperadoes, pirates, taking all, giving little. They came, for the most part, from sordid homes, yet they knew, in some miraculous way, all the fine arts that Paula knew and practised. They were corsetless, pliant, bewildering, lovely, dangerous. They ate lunches that were horrible mixtures of cloying sweets and biting acids yet their skin was like velvet and cream. Their voices were thin, nasal, vulgar; their faces like those in a Greuze or a Fragonard. They said, with a twang that racked the listener, “I wouldn’t of went if I got an invite but he could of give me a ring, anyways. I called him right. I was sore.”

===Chinese===
这些姑娘和在他办公室里做工的那些姑娘之间，存在着一种令德克吃惊又好笑的相似。他对一个纤细的年轻造物说「记一封信，罗奇小姐（Miss Roach）」——这造物同他前一天一起跳舞的那位姑娘一样精致；或者说，同他骑马出游、打网球、打桥牌的那些姑娘一样。她们的衣着本身就是毫无破绽的仿品。她们甚至用同一种香水。他懒懒地纳闷，她们是怎么做到的。她们十八岁、十九岁、二十岁，她们的脸蛋、身段、欲念和天生的本钱，使她们置身于商务写字楼里成了一桩悖论、一件荒唐事。然而她们也都能干，用一种机械的路数。她们的差事本就是机械的差事。她们接电话，压揿杆，摁按钮，敲打字机，记名字。她们是些长着十四岁孩童心智的可爱造物。她们的头发亮泽，波纹熨帖，像小小孩的头发那样纤细、光润、娇柔地打着卷。她们怀着蛇一般的狡慧。她们穿好看的小毛衣、扁平的孩子气衣领，配实惠得可笑的袜子和牛津鞋。她们的腿细瘦而结实。她们的嘴嘟着，柔软，粉红，下唇微微向后翻卷，像花瓣似的，像婴儿刚吃饱奶时湿润的小嘴。她们的两眼分得很开，空落落的，却又无所不晓。她们料理私事如同将军用兵。她们冷静、疏远、倨傲。她们把自己的男朋友逼到绝望。她们是土匪、亡命徒、海盗，尽取所有，绝少付出。她们多半出身腌臜人家，却以某种不可思议的方式，把宝拉所通晓并身体力行的那整套高雅艺术学了个全。她们不束胸衣，柔顺，令人迷乱，可爱，危险。她们吃的午餐是腻人的甜和扎口的酸混成的可怕之物，皮肤却像天鹅绒和奶油。她们的嗓音尖细、带鼻音、俗气；她们的脸却像格勒兹（Greuze）或弗拉戈纳尔（Fragonard）画中人的脸。她们用一种硌得听者牙酸的腔调说：「有人下请帖我也不会去的呀，可不管怎么说，他总可以给我打个电话嘛。我把他痛快骂了一顿。气死我了。」

===Original===
“Yeh? Wha’d he say?”

===Chinese===
「嗯？他说啥？」

===Original===
“Oh, he laffed.”

===Chinese===
「哦，他乐了。」

===Original===
“Didja go?”

===Chinese===
「你去没去？」

===Original===
“Me! No! Whatcha think I yam, anyway?”

===Chinese===
「我！才不呢！你当俺是啥人呀？」

===Original===
“Oh, he’s a good kid.”

===Chinese===
「哦，他是个好小子。」

===Original===
Among these Dirk worked immune, aloof, untouched. He would have been surprised to learn that he was known among them as Frosty. They approved his socks, his scarfs, his nails, his features, his legs in their well-fitting pants, his flat strong back in the Peel coat. They admired and resented him. Not one that did not secretly dream of the day when he would call her into his office, shut the door, and say, “Loretta” (their names were burbankian monstrosities, born of grafting the original appellation onto their own idea of beauty in nomenclature﻿—hence Loretta, Imogene, Nadine, Natalie, Ardella), “Loretta, I have watched you for a long, long time and you must have noticed how deeply I admire you.”

===Chinese===
德克置身这些人中间工作，免疫，超然，不受触动。倘若知道她们背地里管他叫「霜冻」（Frosty），他准会吃一惊。她们赞许他的袜子、他的围巾、他的指甲、他的五官、他穿合身长裤的两条腿、他裹在皮尔外套里平整结实的背。她们又倾慕他又忌恨他。没有一个人不偷偷梦想着这样一天：他把她叫进办公室，关上门，说：「洛蕾塔」（她们的名字都是伯班克式的怪胎，是把原有的本名嫁接到她们自己对命名的美感上的产物——于是有了洛蕾塔（Loretta）、伊莫金（Imogene）、纳丁（Nadine）、娜塔莉（Natalie）、阿德拉（Ardella）），「洛蕾塔，我注视你已经很久很久了，你一定注意到了，我多么深深地爱慕着你。」

===Original===
It wasn’t impossible. Those things happen. The movies had taught them that.

===Chinese===
这并非不可能。这种事时有发生。电影早就教给她们了。

===Original===
Dirk, all unconscious of their pitiless, all-absorbing scrutiny, would have been still further appalled to learn how fully aware they were of his personal and private affairs. They knew about Paula, for example. They admired and resented her, too. They were fair in granting her the perfection of her clothes, drew immense satisfaction from the knowledge of their own superiority in the matters of youth and colouring; despised her for the way in which she openly displayed her feeling for him (how they knew this was a miracle and a mystery, for she almost never came into the office and disguised all her telephone talks with him). They thought he was grand to his mother. Selina had been in his office twice, perhaps. On one of these occasions she had spent five minutes chatting sociably with Ethelinda Quinn who had the face of a Da Vinci cherub and the soul of a man-eating shark. Selina always talked to everyone. She enjoyed listening to street car conductors, washwomen, janitors, landladies, clerks, doormen, chauffeurs, policemen. Something about her made them talk. They opened to her as flowers to the sun. They sensed her interest, her liking. As they talked Selina would exclaim, “You don’t say! Well, that’s terrible!” Her eyes would be bright with sympathy.

===Chinese===
德克对她们那种无情的、兼收并蓄的审视浑然不觉，倘若知道她们对他的私事和个人生活了解得多么彻底，他准会更加骇然。比如，她们知道宝拉。她们对她也又仰慕又忌恨。她们公道地承认她衣着无懈可击，又从自知在年轻与气色两项上高她一着之中汲取巨大的满足；她们鄙夷她对他公然表露感情的那副样子（她们怎么知道的，是个奇迹也是桩谜，因为她几乎从不进办公室，同他的电话谈话也一概加以掩饰）。她们认为他待母亲真是好得没话说。塞莉娜来过他的办公室，大概统共两次。其中一回，她花了五分钟同埃塞琳达·奎因（Ethelinda Quinn）亲亲热热地闲谈——此人长着一张达·芬奇小天使的脸，一颗食人鲨的灵魂。塞莉娜总是跟什么人都聊。她爱听电车售票员、洗衣妇、看门人、房东太太、店员、门房、汽车夫、警察说话。她身上有股什么东西叫人肯开口。他们对她敞开，像花儿向太阳。他们觉出她的兴味，她的喜欢。他们讲着的当儿，塞莉娜便惊叹：「真的吗！哎呀，那也太不像话了！」她的眼睛因同情而发亮。

===Original===
Selina had said, on entering Dirk’s office, “My land! I don’t see how you can work among those pretty creatures and not be a sultan. I’m going to ask some of them down to the farm over Sunday.”

===Chinese===
塞莉娜一进德克的办公室就这样说过：「我的天！我不明白，你成天在这么些漂亮人儿中间做工，怎么还能不做个苏丹。我要请她们中间几位，礼拜天下乡到农场去。」

===Original===
“Don’t, Mother! They wouldn’t understand. I scarcely see them. They’re just part of the office equipment.”

===Chinese===
「可别，妈妈！她们不会懂的。我几乎见不着她们。她们不过是办公室设备的一部分。」

===Original===
Afterward, Ethelinda Quinn had passed expert opinion. “Say, she’s got ten times the guts that Frosty’s got. I like her fine. Did you see her terrible hat! But say, it didn’t look funny on her, did it? Anybody else in that getup would look comical, but she’s the kind that could walk off with anything. I don’t know. She’s got what I call an air. It beats style. Nice, too. She said I was a pretty little thing. Can you beat it! At that she’s right. I cer’nly yam.”

===Chinese===
事后，埃塞琳达·奎因发表了行家的意见：「嘿，她的胆子比『霜冻』大上十倍。我喜欢她，真心喜欢。你见着她那顶吓人的帽子了吧！可是说真的，戴在她头上并不显可笑，是不是？换了别人那身行头准像出滑稽戏，可她就是那种人，披条麻袋都压得住场。我说不上来。她有一样东西，我管它叫气派。气派胜过时髦。人也和气。她说我是个漂亮的小东西。你服不服！这话还真说着了。俺可不就是。」

===Original===
All unconscious, “Take a letter, Miss Quinn,” said Dirk half an hour later.

===Chinese===
半个钟头后，德克全然不觉地说道：「奎因小姐，记一封信。」

===Original===
In the midst, then, of this fiery furnace of femininity Dirk walked unscorched. Paula, the North Shore girls, well-bred business and professional women he occasionally met in the course of business, the enticing little nymphs he encountered in his own office, all practised on him their warm and perfumed wiles. He moved among them cool and serene. Perhaps his sudden success had had something to do with this; and his quiet ambition for further success. For he really was accounted successful now, even in the spectacular whirl of Chicago’s meteoric financial constellation. North-side mammas regarded his income, his career, and his future with eyes of respect and wily speculation. There was always a neat little pile of invitations in the mail that lay on the correct little console in the correct little apartment ministered by the correct little Jap on the correct north-side street near (but not too near) the lake, and overlooking it.

===Chinese===
就这样，德克行走在这座女性的烈焰洪炉之中，毫发未焦。宝拉，北岸的姑娘们，业务往来中偶尔遇见的那些有教养的商界职业妇女，他自家办公室里那些撩人的小宁芙（nymph），都对他施展开温香暖玉、脂粉氤氲的手段。他在她们中间来去，冷静而安详。也许他的骤然成功于此不无干系；还有他为更进一步的成功怀着的静默野心。因为他如今真算得上功成名就了，即便在芝加哥那流星般腾跃的金融星座的壮观旋涡里。城北的妈妈们用敬重而狡黠盘算的眼光掂量他的进项、他的事业、他的前程。邮件里总有一叠齐齐整整的请帖，摆在那张得体的小玄关桌上；桌子在那套得体的小公寓里；公寓由一个得体的日本小男仆打理，坐落在一条得体的城北街上，近着湖（但也不太近），又俯瞰着湖。

===Original===
The apartment had been furnished with Paula’s aid. Together she and Dirk had gone to interior decorators. “But you’ve got to use your own taste, too,” Paula had said, “to give it the individual touch.” The apartment was furnished in a good deal of Italian furniture, the finish a dark oak or walnut, the whole massive and yet somehow unconvincing. The effect was sombre without being impressive. There were long carved tables on which an ash tray seemed a desecration; great chairs roomy enough for lolling, yet in which you did not relax; dull silver candlesticks; vestments; Dante’s saturnine features sneering down upon you from a correct cabinet. There were not many books. Tiny foyer, large living room, bedroom, dining room, kitchen, and a cubbyhole for the Jap. Dirk did not spend much time in the place. Sometimes he did not sit in a chair in the sitting room for days at a time, using the room only as a shortcut in his rush for the bedroom to change from office to dinner clothes. His upward climb was a treadmill, really. His office, the apartment, a dinner, a dance. His contacts were monotonous, and too few. His office was a great splendid office in a great splendid office building in LaSalle Street. He drove back and forth in a motor car along the boulevards. His social engagements lay north. LaSalle Street bounded him on the west, Lake Michigan on the east, Jackson Boulevard on the south, Lake Forest on the north. He might have lived a thousand miles away for all he knew of the rest of Chicago﻿—the mighty, roaring, sweltering, pushing, screaming, magnificent hideous steel giant that was Chicago.

===Chinese===
公寓的布置得力于宝拉。她和德克一起去见过室内装饰商。「不过你也得用自己的趣味，」宝拉说过，「好给它添上个人的点睛之笔。」公寓里陈设了相当多的意大利家具，深色的橡木或胡桃木饰面，整套装得厚重，却又不知怎么总不能令人信服。效果是阴沉而不见庄严。长长的雕花桌上，搁一只烟灰碟都像一种亵渎；宽大的椅子尽容得下懒洋洋的歪靠，你却不肯在里头放松；暗沉的银烛台；法衣绣饰；一个得体的柜子上头，但丁那阴郁的面容居高临下地对你冷笑。书没有几本。小小的门厅，大客厅，卧室，餐室，厨房，还有一格供日本小男仆住的小间。德克在这地方花的工夫不多。有时他一连几天不在客厅的椅子里坐一坐，那间屋子只被他当作抄近路的过道，在赶着从办公室装束换成晚餐装束的匆促里一穿而过。他的向上爬，其实是踩一架踏车。办公室，公寓，一顿晚宴，一场舞。他的交往单调，又太少。他的办公室是拉萨尔街（LaSalle Street）一座宏大辉煌的写字楼里一间宏大辉煌的办公室。他坐着汽车沿条条林荫大道来来去去。他的社交应酬全在北边。拉萨尔街在西面框住他，密歇根湖在东面，杰克逊林荫大道（Jackson Boulevard）在南面，莱克福里斯特在北面。芝加哥其余的部分——那伟岸、咆哮、闷热、推搡、尖叫、壮丽而又丑陋的钢铁巨人芝加哥——他之所知，还不如远在一千英里之外。

===Original===
Selina had had no hand in the furnishing of his apartment. When it was finished Dirk had brought her in triumph to see it. “Well,” he had said, “what do you think of it, Mother?”

===Chinese===
公寓的陈设，塞莉娜没有插过手。布置停当之后，德克得意洋洋地带她来看。「怎么样，」他说，「妈妈，你觉得它如何？」

===Original===
She had stood in the centre of the room, a small plain figure in the midst of these massive sombre carved tables, chairs, chests. A little smile had quirked the corner of her mouth. “I think it’s as cosy as a cathedral.”

===Chinese===
她站在房间中央，一个小小的素净身影，置身于这些厚重阴沉的雕花桌椅柜箱之间。一丝微笑牵起她的嘴角。「我觉得它跟座大教堂一样暖和惬意。」

===Original===
Sometimes Selina remonstrated with him, though of late she had taken on a strange reticence. She no longer asked him about the furnishings of the houses he visited (Italian villas on Ohio Street), or the exotic food he ate at splendid dinners. The farm flourished. The great steel mills and factories to the south were closing in upon her but had not yet set iron foot on her rich green acres. She was rather famous now for the quality of her farm products and her pens. You saw “DeJong asparagus” on the menu at the Blackstone and the Drake hotels. Sometimes Dirk’s friends twitted him about this and he did not always acknowledge that the similarity of names was not a coincidence.

===Chinese===
有时塞莉娜也规劝他几句，尽管近来她添了一种奇怪的缄默。她不再问他去的那些人家屋里如何陈设——俄亥俄街（Ohio Street）上的意大利式别墅——也不问他在盛筵上吃到的异邦吃食。农场兴旺。南边的大钢铁厂大工厂正朝她围拢过来，却还没把铁脚踏上她那油绿丰饶的田亩。如今她农场出产与畜栏牲口的品质已颇有几分名气。你在布莱克斯通（Blackstone）旅馆和德雷克（Drake）旅馆的菜单上能见到「德容芦笋」。有时德克的朋友们拿这个打趣他，而他并不总是肯承认这姓氏相同并非巧合。

===Original===
“Dirk, you seem to see no one but just these people,” Selina told him in one of her infrequent rebukes. “You don’t get the full flavour of life. You’ve got to have a vulgar curiosity about people and things. All kinds of people. All kinds of things. You revolve in the same little circle, over and over and over.”

===Chinese===
「德克，你好像除了这一路人谁也不见似的，」塞莉娜在她难得的责备中的一回说道。「你尝不着人生的全滋味。你得对人对事有一种不惮粗俗的好奇。各色的人。各色的事。你在同一个小圈子里转，转了一圈又一圈又一圈。」

===Original===
“Haven’t time. Can’t afford to take the time.”

===Chinese===
「没工夫。这工夫我花不起。」

===Original===
“You can’t afford not to.”

===Chinese===
「不花才更花不起。」

===Original===
Sometimes Selina came into town for a week or ten days at a stretch, and indulged in what she called an orgy. At such times Julie Arnold would invite her to occupy one of the guest rooms at the Arnold house, or Dirk would offer her his bedroom and tell her that he would be comfortable on the big couch in the living room, or that he would take a room at the University Club. She always declined. She would take a room in a hotel, sometimes north, sometimes south. Her holiday before her she would go off roaming gaily as a small boy on a Saturday morning, with the day stretching gorgeously and adventuresomely ahead of him, sallies down the street without plan or appointment, knowing that richness in one form or another lies before him for the choosing. She loved the Michigan Boulevard and State Street shop windows in which haughty waxed ladies in glittering evening gowns postured, fingers elegantly crooked as they held a fan, a rose, a programme, meanwhile smiling condescendingly out upon an envious world flattening its nose against the plate glass barrier. A sociable woman, Selina, savouring life, she liked the lights, the colour, the rush, the noise. Her years of grinding work, with her face pressed down to the very soil itself, had failed to kill her zest for living. She prowled into the city’s foreign quarters﻿—Italian, Greek, Chinese, Jewish. She penetrated the Black Belt, where Chicago’s vast and growing Negro population shifted and moved and stretched its great limbs ominously, reaching out and out in protest and overflowing the bounds that irked it. Her serene face and her quiet manner, her bland interest and friendly look protected her. They thought her a social worker, perhaps; one of the uplifters. She bought and read the *Independent*, the Negro newspaper in which herb doctors advertised magic roots. She even sent the twenty-five cents required for a box of these, charmed by their names﻿—Adam and Eve roots, Master of the Woods, Dragon’s Blood, High John the Conqueror, Jezebel Roots, Grains of Paradise.

===Chinese===
有时塞莉娜一连进城一礼拜或十天，纵情于她所谓的狂欢。这种时候，朱莉·阿诺德会请她去住阿诺德家的一间客房，或是德克把自己的卧室让给她，说自己睡客厅那张大沙发也很舒服，或者说到大学俱乐部（University Club）去开个房间。她一概谢绝。她自己上旅馆开房间，有时在北边，有时在南边。假期摆在面前，她便快快活活地漫游去，像个星期六早晨的小男孩，一整天的锦绣光景带着奇遇在前头铺展，沿街信步出击，没有计划也不赴约，只知道五花八门的好东西就在前头，任他挑拣。她喜欢密歇根林荫大道和州街（State Street）的商店橱窗，里头上了蜡的傲慢女子穿着亮闪闪的晚礼服摆姿势，手指优雅地弯着，执一柄扇子、一朵玫瑰、一张节目单，一面屈尊俯就地微笑着，俯视那个把鼻子扁在厚玻璃屏障外艳羡张望的世界。塞莉娜是个好交际的女人，细细品味人生，她喜欢灯火、色彩、奔忙、喧闹。那些年磨人的苦工，脸几乎贴到泥土本身，终究没能磨杀她生活的兴头。她潜入城中外国人的地界——意大利人的、希腊人的、华人的、犹太人的。她深入黑带区（Black Belt），芝加哥那庞大而日增的黑人种群正在那里迁动、挪移，不祥地伸展着巨大的肢体，出于抗议向外、再向外探伸，漫过了那些惹它烦躁的界栏。她那安详的脸、沉静的举止、她那温淡的兴趣和友好的眼色护住了她。他们大约当她是个社会服务人员；热心教化人士中的一员。她买来读《独立报》（*Independent*），那份黑人报纸，草药郎中们在上面登着魔根的广告。她甚至照价寄去两毛五分钱，邮购了一盒，被那些名字迷住了——亚当夏娃根（Adam and Eve roots）、林木之主（Master of the Woods）、龙血（Dragon’s Blood）、征服者高约翰（High John the Conqueror）、耶洗别根（Jezebel Roots）、天堂谷粒（Grains of Paradise）。

===Original===
“Look here, Mother,” Dirk would protest, “you can’t wander around like that. It isn’t safe. This isn’t High Prairie, you know. If you want to go round I’ll get Saki to drive you.”

===Chinese===
「你听着，妈妈，」德克总要抗议，「你不能就这么四处乱逛。这不安全。这儿可不是高草原，你知道。你要是想出去转，我叫萨基（Saki）开车送你。」

===Original===
“That would be nice,” she said, mildly. But she never availed herself of this offer. Sometimes she went over to South Water Street, changed now, and swollen to such proportions that it threatened to burst its confines. She liked to stroll along the crowded sidewalks, lined with crates and boxes and barrels of fruits, vegetables, poultry. Swarthy foreign faces predominated now. Where the red-faced overalled men had been she now saw lean muscular lads in old army shirts and khaki pants and scuffed puttees wheeling trucks, loading boxes, charging down the street in huge rumbling auto vans. Their faces were hard, their talk terse. They moved gracefully, with an economy of gesture. Any one of these, she reflected, was more vital, more native, functioned more usefully and honestly than her successful son, Dirk DeJong.

===Chinese===
「那敢情好，」她温和地说。可她从没叨过这份光。有时她去南水街，如今那里变了样，膨胀得眼看要撑破它的边界。她喜欢沿着拥挤的人行道闲逛，路边排满一箱箱一篓篓的水果、蔬菜、家禽。如今深色的外国面孔占了多数。从前那些红脸膛、背带工装的汉子们待过的地方，如今她看见精瘦壮实的小伙子，旧军衬衫、卡其裤、磨旧的绑腿，推着货车，装着箱子，驾着轰隆隆的大汽车货车沿街冲去。他们的脸刚硬，言语简短。他们动作漂亮，一举一动绝不多费。她想，这些人里随便哪一个，都比她那功成名就的儿子德克·德容更有生气、更本色，起的作用更实在，也更诚实。

===Original===
“Where ’r’ beans?”

===Chinese===
「豆子搁哪儿？」

===Original===
“In th’ ol’ beanery.”

===Chinese===
「在老豆铺里。」

===Original===
“Tough.”

===Chinese===
「老得嚼不动。」

===Original===
“Best you can get.”

===Chinese===
「能弄到的最好的了。」

===Original===
“Keep ’em.”

===Chinese===
「留着吧。」

===Original===
Many of the older men knew her, shook hands with her, chatted a moment friendlily. William Talcott, a little more dried up, more wrinkled, his sparse hair quite gray now, still leaned up against the side of his doorway in his shirt sleeves and his neat pepper-and-salt pants and vest, a pretty good cigar, unlighted, in his mouth, the heavy gold watch chain spanning his middle.

===Chinese===
许多上了年纪的人都认得她，跟她握手，亲亲热热聊上一两句。威廉·塔尔科特更干瘦了些，皱纹更深了些，稀疏的头发如今全灰了，仍旧衬衫袖筒靠在自家门框边，穿一条整洁的椒盐呢长裤和马甲，嘴里一支蛮不坏的雪茄，没点着，粗金表链横在腰腹间。

===Original===
“Well, you certainly made good, Mrs. DeJong. Remember the day you come here with your first load?”

===Chinese===
「嘿，你可真熬出头了，德容太太。还记得你拉着头一车货上这儿来的那天不？」

===Original===
Oh, yes. She remembered.

===Chinese===
哦，记得。她记得。

===Original===
“That boy of yours has made his mark, too, I see. Doing grand, ain’t he? Wa-al, great satisfaction having a son turn out well like that. Yes, sirree! Why, look at my da’ter Car’line﻿—”

===Chinese===
「你那小子也成器了，我瞧见啦。干得红火着呢，是不是？嗐，养个儿子这么出息，多舒心哪。可不是嘛！你看俺闺女卡罗琳——」

===Original===
Life at High Prairie had its savour, too. Frequently you saw strange visitors there for a week or ten days at a time﻿—boys and girls whose city pallor gave way to a rich tan; tired-looking women with sagging figures who drank Selina’s cream and ate her abundant vegetables and tender chickens as though they expected these viands to be momentarily snatched from them. Selina picked these up in odd corners of the city. Dirk protested against this, too. Selina was a member of the High Prairie school board now. She often drove about the roads and into town in a disreputable Ford which she manipulated with imagination and skill. She was on the Good Roads Committee and the Truck Farmers’ Association valued her opinion. Her life was full, pleasant, prolific.


===Chinese===
高草原的日子也有它的滋味。你时常看见些生客在那儿一住一礼拜或十天——城里来的姑娘小伙，苍白的脸色褪成了红褐色；疲惫相的女人们，身段松垮，喝着塞莉娜的奶油，吃着她丰盛的蔬菜和嫩鸡，那副吃相，仿佛时刻防着这些吃食会被人从嘴边抢走。这些都是塞莉娜从城市的犄角旮旯里捡来的。连这个德克也反对。塞莉娜如今是高草原学董会的一员了。她常驾着一辆寒碜的福特车（Ford）跑乡下道、进芝加哥城，那车被她摆弄得富于想象而又精熟。她在道路改良委员会（Good Roads Committee）任职，商品菜农公会（Truck Farmers’ Association）也重视她的意见。她的日子过得充实、适意、丰饶多产。

## XVIII / 第十八章

===Original===
Paula had a scheme for interesting women in bond buying. It was a good scheme. She suggested it so that Dirk thought he had thought of it. Dirk was head now of the bond department in the Great Lakes Trust Company’s magnificent new white building on Michigan Boulevard north. Its white towers gleamed pink in the lake mists. Dirk said it was a terrible building, badly proportioned, and that it looked like a vast vanilla sundae. His new private domain was more like a splendid bookless library than a business office. It was finished in rich dull walnut and there were great upholstered chairs, soft rugs, shaded lights. Special attention was paid to women clients. There was a room for their convenience fitted with low restful chairs and couches, lamps, writing desks, in mauve and rose. Paula had selected the furnishings for this room. Ten years earlier it would have been considered absurd in a suite of business offices. Now it was a routine part of the equipment.

===Chinese===
宝拉有个让女人们对买债券发生兴味的方略。这方略不坏。她提出来时拿捏得恰到好处，倒叫德克以为是他自己想出来的。如今德克已是五大湖信托公司债券部的主管，公司在北密歇根林荫大道新起的那座堂皇的白色大楼里。楼中白塔在湖雾里泛着粉色。德克说这楼糟透了，比例失当，活像一大客香草圣代。他这方新的私人领地，与其说是商务办公室，不如说更像一座没有藏书的华丽图书馆。室内镶着深色的胡桃木，摆着宽大的软垫扶手椅、柔软的地毯、罩着纱罩的灯。女客户受到特殊照拂。有一间为她们方便设置的小间，配着低矮安逸的椅子和长沙发、灯具、写字台，一律淡紫与玫瑰两色。这间屋子的陈设是宝拉挑的。十年前，在一套商务办公室里这样一间屋子会被当作荒唐事。如今它已是常规设备的一部分了。

===Original===
Dirk’s private office was almost as difficult of access as that of the nation’s executive. Cards, telephones, office boys, secretaries stood between the caller and Dirk DeJong, head of the bond department. You asked for him, uttering his name in the ear of the six-foot statuesque detective who, in the guise of usher, stood in the centre of the marble rotunda eyeing each visitor with a coldly appraising gaze. This one padded softly ahead of you on rubber heels, only to give you over to the care of a glorified office boy who took your name. You waited. He returned. You waited. Presently there appeared a young woman with inquiring eyebrows. She conversed with you. She vanished. You waited. She reappeared. You were ushered into Dirk DeJong’s large and luxurious inner office. And there formality fled.

===Chinese===
德克的私人办公室，其难进的程度几乎不下于合众国总统的那间。名片、电话、办公室杂役、秘书，层层拦在来客与债券部主管德克·德容之间。你要见他，先把他的名字说给那位六英尺高、雕像般的探员听——此人扮作引座员的模样，立在大理石圆厅正中，用冷冷的估量的目光扫视每一位来客。这位在胶底鞋上放轻了脚步，无声地在你前头引路，却只是把你交给一个升了格的办公室杂役，由他记下你的名姓。你等着。他回来了。你等着。不一会儿出现了一位扬着疑问眉毛的年轻女子。她同你攀谈。她隐去了。你等着。她又出现了。你被引进德克·德容宽大而奢华的里间办公室。而一到那里，拘礼便逃得无影无踪。

===Original===
Dirk was glad to see you; quietly, interestedly glad to see you. As you stated your business he listened attentively, as was his charming way. The volume of business done with women clients by the Great Lakes Trust Company was enormous. Dirk was conservative, helpful﻿—and he always got the business. He talked little. He was amazingly effective. Ladies in the modish black of recent bereavement made quite a sombre procession to his door. His suggestions (often originating with Paula) made the Great Lakes Trust Company’s discreet advertising rich in results. Neat little pamphlets written for women on the subjects of saving, investments. “You are not dealing with a soulless corporation,” said these brochures. “May we serve you? You need more than friends. Before acting, you should have your judgment vindicated by an organization of investment specialists. You may have relatives and friends, some of whom would gladly advise you on investments. But perhaps you rightly feel that the less they know about your financial affairs, the better. To handle trusts, and to care for the securities of widows and orphans, is our business.”

===Chinese===
德克见到你很高兴；不出声地、兴致盎然地见到你很高兴。你说明来意时，他专注地听着，这是他讨人喜欢的一贯做派。五大湖信托公司同女客户做的生意，量是大得惊人的。德克稳健，肯帮衬——而生意总归他做成了。他话不多。他效力惊人。新近居丧、一身时兴黑衣的太太们，到他门前排成一支颇为阴郁的行列。他的种种献策（常发轫于宝拉）使五大湖信托公司那含蓄的广告成效丰硕。专为女性写的储蓄与投资小册子，印得干干净净。「您打交道的不是一具没有灵魂的公司法人，」这些小册子说。「容我们为您效力吗？您需要的比朋友更多。行动之前，您应当让自己的判断得到一家投资专家机构的印证。您也许有亲戚朋友，其中不乏乐于指点您投资的人。但也许您理所当然地觉得，他们对您的财务内情知道得越少越好。经管信托，照管寡妇孤儿的证券，正是我们的本行。」

===Original===
It was startling to note how this sort of thing mounted into millions. “Women are becoming more and more used to the handling of money,” Paula said, shrewdly. “Pretty soon their patronage is going to be as valuable as that of men. The average woman doesn’t know about bonds﻿—about bond buying. They think they’re something mysterious and risky. They ought to be educated up to it. Didn’t you say something, Dirk, about classes in finance for women? You could make a sort of semi-social affair of it. Send out invitations and get various bankers﻿—big men, whose names are known﻿—to talk to these women.”

===Chinese===
这类事情滚成百万千万之数，快得叫人吃惊。「女人们越来越惯于经手钱了，」宝拉精明地说。「要不了多久，她们的惠顾就会同男人们的同样值钱。寻常女人不懂债券——不懂买债券这回事。她们以为那是某种神秘兮兮、冒险兮兮的东西。她们该在这方面受受教育。德克，你不是说过要给女人们开财经课来着？你可以把它办成一种半社交的活动。发请帖，请几位银行家——大人物，名字叫得响的——来给这些女人讲讲。」

===Original===
“But would the women come?”

===Chinese===
「可女人们肯来吗？」

===Original===
“Of course they’d come. Women will accept any invitation that’s engraved on heavy cream paper.”

===Chinese===
「当然肯来。凡是用厚实的米色纸印了凸版字的请帖，女人们来者不拒。」

===Original===
The Great Lakes Trust had a branch in Cleveland now, and one in New York, on Fifth Avenue. The drive to interest women in bond buying and to instruct them in finance was to take on almost national proportions. There was to be newspaper and magazine advertising.

===Chinese===
五大湖信托如今在克利夫兰（Cleveland）开了一家分行，又在纽约第五大道开了一家。这番使女人对买债券发生兴味、教她们通晓财经的鼓动，眼看要闹成近乎全国的局面。报纸杂志上还要登广告。

===Original===
The Talks for Women on the Subject of Finance were held every two weeks in the crystal room of the Blackstone and were a great success. Paula was right. Much of old Aug Hempel’s shrewdness and business foresight had descended to her. The women came﻿—widows with money to invest; business women who had thriftily saved a portion of their salaries; moneyed women who wanted to manage their own property, or who resented a husband’s interference. Some came out of curiosity. Others for lack of anything better to do. Others to gaze on the well-known banker or lawyer or business man who was scheduled to address the meeting. Dirk spoke three or four times during the winter and was markedly a favourite. The women, in smart crêpe gowns and tailored suits and small chic hats, twittered and murmured about him, even while they sensibly digested his well-thought-out remarks. He looked very handsome, clean-cut, and distinguished there on the platform in his admirably tailored clothes, a small white flower in his buttonhole. He talked easily, clearly, fluently; answered the questions put to him afterward with just the right mixture of thoughtful hesitation and confidence.

===Chinese===
「女性财经讲座」（Talks for Women on the Subject of Finance）每两周在布莱克斯通旅馆的水晶厅举行一次，大获成功。宝拉说得不错。老奥格·亨佩尔的精明和生意远见，很传了她一份。女人们来了——手里有钱待投资的寡妇；从薪水里省吃俭用攒下一份的职业妇女；想亲自打理自己财产的有钱太太，或是恼恨丈夫越权插手的。有的是出于好奇来的。有的因为再没有更好的事可做。还有的是为一睹那位排定演讲的知名银行家、律师或实业家的丰采。一冬之间德克讲了三四次，分明是个红人。那些穿时新绉纱长裙、裁制套装、别致小帽的女人们，围着他嘁嘁喳喳、交头接耳，一面却又明明白白地消化着他那深思熟虑的讲辞。他站在讲台上，衣装裁剪得无可挑剔，钮孔里簪一朵小白花，看去十分英俊、干净、出众。他讲得从容、清晰、滔滔不绝；随后回答提问时，深思的沉吟与自信又掺和得恰到好处。

===Original===
It was decided that for the national advertising there must be an illustration that would catch the eye of women, and interest them. The person to do it, Dirk thought, was this Dallas O’Mara whose queer hen-track signature you saw scrawled on half the advertising illustrations that caught your eye. Paula had not been enthusiastic about this idea.

===Chinese===
于是议定，全国性广告须配一幅能抓住女人眼目、勾起她们兴味的插图。执笔的人，德克想，非这位达拉斯·奥马拉（Dallas O'Mara）莫属——凡是惹你注目的广告插图，半数都涂着她那母鸡刨食似的怪签名。宝拉可不曾对这个主意热心过。

===Original===
“M-m-m, she’s very good,” Paula had said, guardedly, “but aren’t there others who are better?”

===Chinese===
「嗯——嗯，她是很不错，」宝拉曾留有余地地说，「可难道没有比她更好的吗？」

===Original===
“She!” Dirk had exclaimed. “Is it a woman? I didn’t know. That name might be anything.”

===Chinese===
「她！」德克当时失声道。「是个女人？我不知道。这名字听着什么都像。」

===Original===
“Oh, yes, she’s a woman. She’s said to be very﻿—very attractive.”

===Chinese===
「哦，是的，她是个女人。据说人非常——非常有魅力。」

===Original===
Dirk sent for Dallas O’Mara. She replied, suggesting an appointment two weeks from that date. Dirk decided not to wait, consulted other commercial artists, looked at their work, heard their plans outlined, and was satisfied with none of them. The time was short. Ten days had passed. He had his secretary call Dallas O’Mara on the telephone. Could she come down to see him that day at eleven?

===Chinese===
德克派人去请达拉斯·奥马拉。她回话说，不如把会面约在两星期之后。德克决定不等了，请教了别的商业画家，看了他们的作品，听了他们概述的方案，一个也没看上。时间紧了。十天过去了。他叫秘书打电话给达拉斯·奥马拉。她当天十一点能不能来见他一面？

===Original===
No: she worked until four daily at her studio.

===Chinese===
不成：她每天在画室要画到四点。

===Original===
Could she come to his office at four-thirty, then?

===Chinese===
那么四点半到他的办公室来，行吗？

===Original===
Yes, but wouldn’t it be better if he could come to her studio where he could see something of the various types of drawings﻿—oils, or black-and-white, or crayons. She was working mostly in crayons now.

===Chinese===
行，可要是他能上她画室来岂不更好，在那儿他可以看看各式画作——油画啦，黑白画啦，色粉画啦。她如今主要画色粉画。

===Original===
All this relayed by his secretary at the telephone to Dirk at his desk. He jammed his cigarette-end viciously into a tray, blew a final infuriated wraith of smoke, and picked up the telephone connection on his own desk. “One of those damned temperamental near-artists trying to be grand,” he muttered, his hand over the mouthpiece. “Here, Miss Rawlings﻿—I’ll talk to her. Switch her over.”

===Chinese===
这一切由他的秘书在电话里转述给坐在办公桌前的德克。他把烟头狠狠摁进烟盘，喷出最后一缕怒气冲冲的青烟，抓起自己桌上的电话。「又一个这路可恶的、耍艺术家脾气的半吊子，还想摆谱，」他手捂着话筒咕哝道。「喂，罗林斯小姐（Miss Rawlings）——我来跟她讲。给我转过去。」

===Original===
“Hello, Miss﻿—uh﻿—O’Mara. This is Mr. DeJong talking. I much prefer that you come to my office and talk to me.” (No more of this nonsense.)

===Chinese===
「喂，小姐——呃——奥马拉。我是德容先生。我更希望你到我的办公室来面谈。」（这路胡闹，到此为止。）

===Original===
Her voice: “Certainly, if you prefer it. I thought the other would save us both some time. I’ll be there at four-thirty.” Her voice was leisurely, low, rounded. An admirable voice. Restful.

===Chinese===
她的声音：「当然可以，要是您喜欢这样。我原以为那一种能省咱们两人一点时间。我四点半到。」她的声音从容、低沉、圆润。一副好嗓子。听着安逸。

===Original===
“Very well. Four-thirty,” said Dirk, crisply. Jerked the receiver onto the hook. That was the way to handle ’em. These females of forty with straggling hair and a bundle of drawings under their arm.

===Chinese===
「很好。四点半，」德克干脆利落地说，咔地把听筒撂回挂钩。对付这路人就得这样。这些四十岁的女人，头发披散着，胳膊底下夹着一卷画。

===Original===
The female of forty with straggling hair and a bundle of drawings under her arm was announced at four-thirty to the dot. Dirk let her wait five minutes in the outer office, being still a little annoyed. At four-thirty-five there entered his private office a tall slim girl in a smart little broadtail jacket, fur-trimmed skirt, and a black hat at once so daring and so simple that even a man must recognize its French nativity. She carried no portfolio of drawings under her arms.

===Chinese===
四点半整，那个头发披散、胳膊底下夹着一卷画的四十岁女人被通报到。德克余怒未消，让她在外间办公室坐了五分钟。四点三十五分，走进他私人办公室的是一位修长苗条的姑娘，穿一件时新的宽尾羔皮（broadtail）小外套、镶皮边的裙子，戴一顶黑帽子——那帽子又大胆又简净，叫一个男人也认得出它的法兰西出身。她胳膊底下并没有什么画夹。

===Original===
Through the man’s mind flashed a series of unbusinesslike thoughts such as: “Gosh!﻿ ﻿… Eyes!﻿ ﻿… That’s way I like to see girl dress﻿ ﻿… Tired looking﻿ ﻿… No, guess it’s her eyes﻿—sort of fatigued.﻿ ﻿… Pretty﻿ ﻿… No, she isn’t﻿ ﻿… yes, she﻿ ﻿…” Aloud he said, “This is very kind of you, Miss O’Mara.” Then he thought that sounded pompous and said, curtly, “Sit down.”

===Chinese===
这男人脑中电闪般掠过一串不像谈生意的念头，比如：「天哪！……这双眼睛！……姑娘家就要这样穿才对味……一脸倦容……不对，想是她的眼睛——有点疲。……标致……不，算不上……是，她……」出口的话却是：「您肯来，真是太好了，奥马拉小姐。」随即又觉得这话听着浮夸，便生硬地说：「坐。」

===Original===
Miss O’Mara sat down. Miss O’Mara looked at him with her tired deep blue eyes. Miss O’Mara said nothing. She regarded him pleasantly, quietly, composedly. He waited for her to say that usually she did not come to business offices; that she had only twenty minutes to give him; that the day was warm, or cold; his office handsome; the view over the river magnificent. Miss O’Mara said nothing, pleasantly. So Dirk began to talk, rather hurriedly.

===Chinese===
奥马拉小姐坐下了。奥马拉小姐用她那双疲惫的深蓝色眼睛望着他。奥马拉小姐一言不发。她愉快地、安静地、沉着地打量他。他等着她说，她向来是不进商务写字间的；她只能拨给他二十分钟；今天天热，或者天冷；他的办公室气派；窗外河上的景致壮观。奥马拉小姐愉快地一言不发。于是德克开了腔，说得颇为急促。

===Original===
Now, this was a new experience for Dirk DeJong. Usually women spoke to him first and fluently. Quiet women waxed voluble under his silence; voluble women chattered. Paula always spoke a hundred words to his one. But here was a woman more silent than he; not sullenly silent, nor heavily silent, but quietly, composedly, restfully silent.

===Chinese===
这情形，对德克·德容可是桩新鲜体验。往常总是女人们先开口，而且滔滔不绝。娴静的女人在他的沉默底下变得健谈；健谈的女人叽叽喳喳。宝拉对他的一句话，永远要回上一百句。可眼前这个女人比他还沉默；不是愠愠的沉默，也不是沉甸甸的沉默，而是安安静静、沉着自若、叫人心定的沉默。

===Original===
“I’ll tell you the sort of thing we want, Miss O’Mara.” He told her. When he had finished she probably would burst out with three or four plans. The others had done that.

===Chinese===
「我来告诉您我们要的是哪一路东西，奥马拉小姐。」他讲了。他讲完之后，她多半会脱口抛出三四个方案来。别人都是那样。

===Original===
When he had finished she said, “I’ll think about it for a couple of days while I’m working on something else. I always do. I’m doing an olive soap picture now. I can begin work on yours Wednesday.”

===Chinese===
他讲完之后，她说：「我要先琢磨两三天，同时手上做点别的。我一向这样。我这会儿正画一张橄榄皂广告画。您那张，我星期三可以动笔。」

===Original===
“But I’d like to see it﻿—that is, I’d like to have an idea of what you’re planning to do with it.” Did she think he was going to let her go ahead without consulting his judgment!

===Chinese===
「可我想看看——就是说，我想先有个数，您打算把它画成什么样。」难道她以为，他会不问过他的眼力就由着她画下去！

===Original===
“Oh, it will be all right. But drop into the studio if you like. It will take me about a week, I suppose. I’m over on Ontario in that old studio building. You’ll know it by the way most of the bricks have fallen out of the building and are scattered over the sidewalk.” She smiled a slow wide smile. Her teeth were good but her mouth was too big, he thought. Nice big warm kind of smile, though. He found himself smiling, too, sociably. Then he became businesslike again. Very businesslike.

===Chinese===
「哦，错不了。您乐意的话就到画室来坐坐。我想大概要一个星期。我在安大略街（Ontario Street）那边一幢旧画室楼里。您一认就认得——那楼的砖大半都掉下来了，撒得满人行道都是。」她缓缓绽开一个大大的微笑。她牙齿很好，可嘴太大，他想。不过那笑倒是一种大大的、暖烘烘的笑。他发觉自己也应酬地跟着笑了。随后他又摆回生意面孔。十足的生意面孔。

===Original===
“How much do you﻿—what is your﻿—what would you expect to get for a drawing such as that?”

===Chinese===
「您要多少——您的——这么一幅画，您指望要多少钱？」

===Original===
“Fifteen hundred dollars,” said Miss O’Mara.

===Chinese===
「一千五百块，」奥马拉小姐说。

===Original===
“Nonsense.” He looked at her then. Perhaps that had been humour. But she was not smiling. “You mean fifteen hundred for a single drawing?”

===Chinese===
「胡说。」他这才端详她。方才那句没准儿是幽默。可她并没有笑。「您是说，单单一幅画要一千五百？」

===Original===
“For that sort of thing, yes.”

===Chinese===
「那一类的话，是。」

===Original===
“I’m afraid we can’t pay that, Miss O’Mara.”

===Chinese===
「恐怕我们出不起这个数，奥马拉小姐。」

===Original===
Miss O’Mara stood up. “That is my price.” She was not at all embarrassed. He realized that he had never seen such effortless composure. It was he who was fumbling with the objects on his flat-topped desk﻿—a pen, a sheet of paper, a blotter. “Goodbye, Mr. DeJong.” She held out a friendly hand. He took it. Her hair was gold﻿—dull gold, not bright﻿—and coiled in a single great knot at the back of her head, low. He took her hand. The tired eyes looked up at him.

===Chinese===
奥马拉小姐站起身。「这就是我的价钱。」她一点不窘。他意识到，自己从未见过这样毫不费力的沉着。倒是他自己在平顶办公桌的物件间摸索起来——一支笔、一张纸、一块吸墨垫。「再见，德容先生。」她伸出一只友好的手。他握住了。她的头发是金色的——暗金，不亮——在脑后低低地绾成单单一个大髻。他握着她的手。那双疲惫的眼睛仰望着他。

===Original===
“Well, if that’s your price, Miss O’Mara. I wasn’t prepared to pay any such﻿—but of course I suppose you top-notchers do get crazy prices for your work.”

===Chinese===
「好吧，既然这是您的价，奥马拉小姐。我原没预备出这一路——不过当然，我想你们这些顶尖儿的人物，作品卖疯价钱也是常事。」

===Original===
“Not any crazier than the prices you top-notchers get.”

===Chinese===
「再疯也疯不过你们顶尖儿人物挣的价钱。」

===Original===
“Still, fifteen hundred dollars is quite a lot of money.”

===Chinese===
「话虽这么说，一千五百块到底是好大一笔钱。」

===Original===
“I think so, too. But then, I’ll always think anything over nine dollars is quite a lot of money. You see, I used to get twenty-five cents apiece for sketching hats for Gage’s.”

===Chinese===
「我也这么想。不过话又说回来，凡是超过九块钱的东西，我永远都会觉得是好大一笔钱。您知道，我从前给盖奇帽店（Gage's）画帽子速写，一张两毛五。」

===Original===
She was undeniably attractive. “And now you’ve arrived. You’re successful.”

===Chinese===
她确凿无疑地有魅力。「如今您功成名就了。您出息了。」

===Original===
“Arrived! Heavens, no! I’ve started.”

===Chinese===
「功成名就！天哪，哪儿的话！我才刚起步。」

===Original===
“Who gets more money than you do for a drawing?”

===Chinese===
「画一幅画挣的钱，还有谁比您多吗？」

===Original===
“Nobody, I suppose.”

===Chinese===
「我想没有。」

===Original===
“Well, then?”

===Chinese===
「那么——？」

===Original===
“Well, then, in another minute I’ll be telling you the story of my life.”

===Chinese===
「那么，再过一分钟，我就要给您讲我这一辈子的身世了。」

===Original===
She smiled again her slow wide smile; turned to leave. Dirk decided that while most women’s mouths were merely features this girl’s was a decoration.

===Chinese===
她又绽开那缓缓的大大的微笑；转身要走。德克断定，寻常女人的嘴不过是五官之一，这姑娘的嘴却是一件装饰。

===Original===
She was gone. Miss Ethelinda Quinn et al., in the outer office, appraised the costume of Miss Dallas O’Mara from her made-to-order footgear to her made-in-France millinery and achieved a lightning mental reconstruction of their own costumes. Dirk DeJong in the inner office realized that he had ordered a fifteen-hundred-dollar drawing, sight unseen, and that Paula was going to ask questions about it.

===Chinese===
